I recalled the events of the past night, and was pleased to think that I had betrayed myself to no one but Dr. H. I was satisfied with my experiment.
Ah! would that I had been satisfied! Yet history must go on.

===Chinese===

我用无花果投掷那些罕见的异域珍禽，它们金色与绯红色的羽翼在枝桠间扑朔闪烁；我亦用阿拉伯语的温柔昵语将它们哄至身旁。在棕榈林荫大道上，我与哈菲兹（Hafiz）携手漫步，听着时光在他无与伦比的诗篇脉络中如歌流淌。在欢畅的凉亭（kiosks）中，我畅饮着果汁冰糕（sherbet），并在放浪形骸的奢靡中，一滴一滴吻尽信徒视作违禁品的另一种琼浆。此刻，我在香橼树的绿荫下卧下酣睡。当我醒来时，已是清晨——实实在在的清晨，而非大麻的幻象。睁开双眼时我感受到的最初情绪，是因万物重现自然风貌而升起的幸福感。是的；尽管我所感知到的上一段体验似乎满足了人类无论是肉体还是精神上的一切渴求，但我仍微笑着打量寝室朴素平整的四面白墙，以一种毫无转投阿拉伯式繁复纹饰或彩虹怀抱的惬意，向它们那熟悉的质朴致意。这恰似在一群异乡人的宫殿中度过了孤寂的永恒岁月之后重返家园。我完全可以说那是一段永恒，因为在整整一天里，我都无法摆脱自己与前一天相隔了无尽岁月的感觉。事实上，我从未彻底摆脱过这种感觉。

我起身测试自己复苏的力量，看看机能恢复是否彻底。是的，我未感到一丝肉体的疲惫或精神的沮丧。每一项机能都已恢复至常态，唯独上文提及的那个例外：记忆无法抹去我曾历经一场伟大玄奥奥秘的痕迹。我回想起昨夜发生的一切，欣慰地想到自己除H医生外并未向任何人暴露自身的状态。我对自己的实验感到心满意足。

啊！但愿我真能就此满足！然而历史的洪流仍须滚滚向前。

## IV Cashmere and Cathay by Twilight / 第四章 暮色中的克什米尔与契丹
===Original===

“You will never take it again, will you?”
“Oh no, I never expect to; I am satisfied with my one successful experiment.”
It was the fair lady of the crochet-needle who asked me the question as, a few days after my first practical acquaintance with hashish, I gave her the recital contained in the preceding pages. In my answer I spoke truly; I did suppose that I never should repeat my experiment. The glimpse which I had gained in that single night of revelation of hitherto unconceived modes and uncharted fields of spiritual being seemed enough to store the treasure-house of grand memories for a lifetime. Unutterably more, doubtless, still remained unveiled, but it contented me to say,

> “In Nature’s infinite book of secrecy

A little I can read,”

===Chinese===

“你再也不会服食它了，对吧？”
“噢，绝不会了，我绝不指望再碰它；做过这一次成功的实验，我已经心满意足了。”

在我第一次切身体会大麻效力几天之后，当我向那位手握钩针的端庄女士讲述了前文所记述的一切时，她向我提出了这个问题。在回答中，我说的句句属实；我当时确实以为自己绝不会再重复这一实验。在那启示的一夜中，我对迄今未曾设想的精神存在形式以及未绘入图谱的领域所获得的惊鸿一瞥，似乎已足够充实我一生的宏伟记忆宝库。毫无疑问，难以言喻的更多奥秘依然深藏未露，但我已心满意足地念道：

> “在自然无尽的秘密之书中，

我略通一二，”

===Original===

when that little swept a view whose faintest lineament outshone all the characters upon the scroll of daily existence. No, I never should take it again.
I did not know myself; I did not know hashish. There are temperaments, no doubt, upon which this drug produces, as a reactory result, physical and mental depression. With me, this was never the case. Opium and liquors fix themselves as a habit by becoming necessary to supply that nervous waste which they in the first place occasioned. The lassitude which succeeds their exaltation demands a renewed indulgence, and accordingly every gratification of the appetite is parent to the next. But no such element entered into the causes which attached me to hashish. I speak confidently, yet without exaggeration, when I say that I have spent many an hour in torture such as was never known by Cranmer at the stake, or Gaudentio di Lucca in the Inquisition, yet out of the depths of such experience *I* have always come without a trace of its effect in diminished strength or buoyancy.
Had the first experiment been followed by depression, I had probably never repeated it. At any rate, unstrung muscles and an enervated mind could have been resisted much more effectually when they pleaded for renewed indulgence than the form which the fascination actually took. For days I was even unusually strong; all the forces of life were in a state of pleasurable activity, but the memory of the wondrous glories which I had beheld wooed me continually like an irresistible sorceress. I could not shut my eyes for midday musing without beholding in that world, half dark, half light, beneath the eyelids, a steady procession of delicious images which the severest will could not banish nor dim. Now through an immense and serene sky floated luxurious argosies of clouds, continually changing form and tint through an infinite cycle of mutations.
Now, suddenly emerging from some deep embowerment of woods, I stood upon the banks of a broad river that curved far off into dreamy distance, and glided noiselessly past its jutting headlands, reflecting a light which was not of the sun nor of the moon, but midway between them, and here and there thrilling with subdued prismatic rays. Temples and gardens, fountains and vistas stretched continually through my waking or sleeping imagination, and mingled themselves with all I heard, or read, or saw.

===Chinese===

当这“略通一二”所掠过的一瞥，其最微弱的轮廓都令日常生存长卷上的一切字符相形见绌时，我便已足慰平生。不，我绝不会再服食它了。

我并不了解自己；我亦不了解大麻。毫无疑问，在某些体质的人身上，这种药物会引发肉体与精神沮丧的反应后果。对我而言，却从来不是如此。鸦片与烈酒之所以成瘾，是因为它们变成了填补自身最初造成的神经损耗的必需品。狂喜亢奋之后的倦怠需要再次借药消愁，因此每一次食欲欲望的满足，都孕育着下一次的沉沦。但这类因素并未构成将我系于大麻之上的缘由。我可以充满信心地——绝非夸大其词地——断言：我曾有许多个钟头沉沦在火刑柱上的克兰默（Cranmer）或宗教裁判所里的戈登齐奥·迪·卢卡（Gaudentio di Lucca）都未曾领教过的折磨之中；然而，从如此深重的体验深渊中脱身之后，*我*却始终未曾留下哪怕一丝体力衰竭或活力消退的痕迹。

倘若第一次实验之后伴随而来的是抑郁消沉，我或许就绝不会再行尝试了。无论如何，松弛的肌肉与衰竭的心智若乞求再次放纵，要抵御它们远比抵御这股魔力实际采取的形态容易得多。在接下来的数日里，我的体魄甚至异乎寻常地强健；所有生命机能皆处于令人愉悦的活跃状态，然而我对亲眼目睹过的奇妙辉煌的记忆，却如同一位无法抗拒的女巫般，持续不断地向我暗送秋波。哪怕只是在正午阖眼冥想，我都无法不在眼睑下那半明半暗的世界里，看到一队队美妙绝伦的意象络绎不绝地行进，任凭最坚强的意志也无法驱散或削弱它们。时而，穿过广袤宁静的苍穹，飘过一艘艘奢华的云彩巨舰，在无尽的变幻轮回中持续变幻着形貌与色调。

时而，从一片幽深繁茂的林荫深处赫然脱身，我伫立在一座宽阔大河的岸边，河水蜿蜒流向迷离梦幻的远方，无声地掠过突兀的海角岬角，倒映着一种既非日光亦非月色、而是介于两者之间的光芒，并不时因柔和的折射虹彩而荡漾悸动。神殿与园林、喷泉与远景，无休止地穿行于我清醒或沉睡的想象之中，并与我所听到、读到或看到的一切交融在一起。

===Original===

On the pages of Gibbon the palaces and lawns of Nicomedia were illustrated with a hashish tint and a hashish reality; and journeying with old Dan Chaucer, I drank in a delicious landscape of revery along all the road to Canterbury. The music of my vision was still heard in echo; as the bells of Bow of old time called to Whittington, so did it call to me﻿—“Turn again, turn again.” And I turned.
Censure me not harshly, ye who have never known what fascination there is in the ecstasy of beauty; there are baser attractions than those which invited me. Perhaps ye yourselves have turned from the first simple-mindedness of life to be led by the power of a more sordid wooing. The hope of being one day able to sleep lazily in a literally golden sun, the lazzaroni of fortune; of securing a patient hearing for some influential and patriotic whisper in the ear of the “*mobilium turba Quiritium;*” of draining any cup which drugs the soul and leaves the body to rifle it of its prerogative﻿—each and all of these are lower fascinations than that to which I yielded.
And ye better, wiser, and therefore gentler ones, who decry not another’s weakness because it is not your own, who are free from all bondage, be it of the sordid or the beautiful, be kindly in your judgement. Wherein I was wrong I was invited as by a mother’s voice, and the blandishments which lulled me were full of such spiritual sweetness as we hear only twice in a lifetime﻿—once at its opening, once at its close; the first time in the cradle-hymn that lulls innocence to slumber, the last in that music of attendant angels through which the soul begins to float upward in its euthanasia toward the restoration of primeval purity and peace. I yielded to no sensual gratification. The motives for the hashish-indulgence were of the most exalted ideal nature, for of this nature are all its ecstasies and its revelations﻿—yes, and a thousandfold more terrible, for this very reason, its unutterable pangs. I yielded, moreover, without realizing to what. Within a circle of one hundred miles’ radius there was not a living soul who knew or could warn me of my danger. Finally, I yielded without knowing that I yielded, for I ascribed my next indulgence to a desire of research.

===Chinese===

在吉本（Gibbon）的字里行间，尼科米底亚（Nicomedia）的宫殿与草坪皆被染上了大麻的色彩与大麻的真实感；而同老乔叟（Dan Chaucer）结伴同游时，我沿着通往坎特伯雷的整条大道，尽情饱览了沉思冥想的甜美风光。我幻象中的音乐依然余音袅袅；正如昔日保罗钟楼（Bow bells）呼唤惠廷顿（Whittington）那样，它亦向我呼唤：“归来吧，归来吧。”于是我转身归去。

你们这些从未领略过至美狂喜中蕴含何等魔力的人，切莫严苛地责难我；世间还有比召唤我的诱惑更为卑劣的引诱。或许你们自己也曾脱离了生命最初的纯真质朴，被某种更为俗不可耐的诱惑所裹挟。期冀有朝一日能像命运的乞丐（lazzaroni）一样，慵懒地沉睡在字面意义上的金色艳阳下；期冀能在“善变罗马平民的骚乱乌合之众（*mobilium turba Quiritium*）”耳畔吹风，为某种位高权重且冠冕堂皇的私语谋求耐心的聆听；期冀饮尽任何麻醉灵魂并任由肉体洗劫其特权的酒杯——凡此种种，皆是比我所屈从的诱惑更为低俗的诱惑。

而你们这些更优秀、更明智，因而也更为仁厚的人，你们不会因他人的软弱非己所有而妄加贬斥，你们摆脱了一切桎梏，无论其属于庸俗还是属于至美，请在你们的裁决中秉持宽厚。凡我行之有差之处，皆因受到宛如母亲声音般的呼唤，那安抚我的温言软语充盈着唯有在生命中得闻两次的空灵甜美——一次在生命的肇始，一次在生命的终结；第一次是在抚慰无邪心灵的摇篮曲中。

===Original===

One day, about the hour of noon, a little more than a week after my first experiment, I rolled twenty grains of hashish into a pill and swallowed it, saying as I did so, “Here is the final test for the sake of science.” The afternoon lay before me unoccupied by any especial appointment, and, after dining, I threw myself down upon a lounge to await the result of the dose. The day was soft and hazy, and its influence lay so nepenthe-like upon my eyelids, that before long, without knowing it, I fell asleep. It was teatime when I awoke, and I had not experienced any visions. A friend of mine joined me at the table, and when we pushed back our chairs, he proposed that we should take a walk. Everything above, below, around us united in the invitation. It was one of those evenings when the universal sense of balminess makes all outdoors as homelike and delicious as the cheeriest winter fireside can be, with its enlivenment of ruddy blaze, and its charm of sheltered privacy. The very soul seems turned inside out for an airing, and we are almost ashamed of ourselves forever preferring rafters to the sky, and fleeing from the presence of Nature to find a home.
Through all the streets that ran toward the west the sun was sending a thrill of light from his goodbye place on the horizon, and the pavements were a mosaic of dancing leaf-shadows and golden polygons, forever shifting as the trees quivered over us in the gentlest of southern winds. Arm-in-arm with Dan, I strolled down the checkered avenue, and more and more luxuriant grew the sunset as we came gradually out of the environment of houses and breathed the air of the open country. The suburbs of P﻿—﻿— are very beautiful. If the stranger knows it and remarks it, it is not because he is smitten with the mere novelty of his view. There are few landscapes which will bear so frequent beholding﻿—few whose admirers so soon and lastingly become their lovers. Were there any jealousy in my love for that, my own home-scenery, I know no season which would ever have given me more pangs for fear of a rival than the one of which I speak, for the earth and sky were fair around us, even with a human fascination. Of my companion let me say that which any man of varying moods will realize to be one of the highest eulogies that can be passed upon a friend.

===Chinese===

在第一次实验过去一周多之后的某天正午时分，我将二十格令大麻膏揉成一粒药丸吞了下去，一边吞服一边自言自语道：“为了科学，这是最后一次检验。”整个下午我没有任何特别的约定，用过正餐之后，我便仰卧在躺椅上等待药力发作。那天天气柔和朦胧，其抚慰宛若忘忧草（nepenthe）般覆在我的眼睑上，不久之后，我便浑然不觉地睡着了。当我醒来时已是茶歇时分，并未经历任何幻象。我的一位朋友在餐桌旁与我会合，推椅离席之后，他提议一起去散步。头顶、脚下以及身周的一切万物皆融汇成同一声诚挚的相邀。那是怎样的一个黄昏啊：弥漫周遭的温煦芳菲，让户外的一切宛如冬日里最欢畅的壁炉旁一样温馨宜人，带着红润火光的盎然生机与庇护幽居的魅力。整个灵魂仿佛都被翻出晾晒，令我们几乎为自己永远偏爱房梁胜过苍穹、为寻找居所而逃离大自然的怀抱而感到惭愧。

穿过所有向西蜿蜒的街道，落日正从地平线上的告别之处洒下一缕缕令人悸动的光芒；铺石路面上铺陈着晃动的叶影与金色多边形构成的马赛克拼图，随着头顶树木在最轻柔的南风中摇曳而变幻不休。我与丹（Dan）携手漫步在光影斑驳的林荫道上，当我们渐渐脱离密集的民居、呼吸到开阔乡野的清新空气时，晚霞变得愈发绚烂多姿。P市的郊野景色绝美。倘若异乡人深知并赞叹这一点，绝非仅仅是因为被初见的新奇所打动。很少有风光经得起如此频繁的凝望——也很少有倾慕者如此迅速而长久地化作其忠实的情人。倘若在我对自己故土景色的爱慕中存在任何嫉妒心，我不知道还有哪个季节会比我此刻所说的这个时节更因担心竞争对手而令我备受煎熬，因为身周的天地如此娇艳，甚至散发着凡俗人间的无尽魅力。关于我的这位同伴，且容我道出任何心境多变之人皆能领会的、对朋友所能给予的最高赞誉之一。

===Original===

Dan was one of those choice spirits whom you are always glad to have beside you, whatever may be your feeling. He belonged to that rare and sensitive order of beings who can never become uncongenial to one who has once been in sympathy with them. How many a time, most valued and longed-for one, have I tested this in thee! How often, in this very intuitive perception of our accordance, have I felt the proof that friendship is as inborn a principle in hearts as the quality of their harmony in tones of a chord.
There is a road running south from the suburbs of P﻿—﻿— which in many respects affords one of the most delightful walks which can be imagined. On the one hand, for a long distance, a terraced embankment rises luxuriantly green through all the days of summer, and crowned with picturesque rusurban cottages. On the other, a broad tableland stretches away to the abrupt banks of the Hudson, dotted over all its surface with clumps of healthful trees and embowered villas. Here and there, through the fringes of shade which skirt the brink, delicious views of the river break upon the eye, with a background of mountains, still unsubdued by labor, rising in primeval freshness from the other side. Under the tutelar protection of their evening shadows the farther water lay, at the season of which I speak, like a divine child asleep, watched by an eternal nurse.
Along this road we traveled arm-in-arm, so filled and overcome with the beauty of the view that we read each other’s feelings and went silently. Perhaps we had come half a mile from the town when, without the smallest premonition, I was smitten by the hashish thrill as by a thunderbolt. Though I had felt it but once in a life before, its sign was as unmistakable as the most familiar thing of daily life. I have often been asked to explain the nature of this thrill, and have as often tried to do it, but no analogue exists which will represent it perfectly, hardly even approximately. The nearest resemblance to the feeling is that contained in our idea of the instantaneous separation of soul and body. Very few in the world have ever known before absolute death what state accompanies this separation, yet we all of us have an idea more or less distinct of that which it must be when it arrives.

===Chinese===

丹是那种出类拔萃的知己之一，无论你的心情如何，只要他在身旁，你总会感到由衷的欢喜。他属于那样一种罕见而敏锐的人：凡曾与他们心意相通之人，绝无可能再与他们产生隔阂。最珍贵、最切盼的挚友啊，不知有多少次我在你身上印证了这一点！不知有多少次，就在我们这种关于彼此心契的直觉体悟中，我深切感受到了铁证：友谊深植于心底，乃是与生俱来的天性，恰如和弦中各音调相互谐和的质性一般自然。

有一条道路从P市郊区向南延伸，在诸多方面都堪称能想象到的最惬意的散步路线之一。一侧长长延伸着一座梯田状的堤岸，在整个夏日里都郁郁葱葱，顶端点缀着如诗如画的城郊田园别墅。另一侧，开阔的高地一直延展至哈德逊河（Hudson）陡峭的河岸，整片土地上散布着一簇簇生机盎然的树木与掩映在树荫下的别院。在点缀岸边的绿荫边缘，河流的醉人景致不时映入眼帘；对岸尚未被人工劳作驯服的山峦巍然耸立，散发着太古的苍翠气息，构成了宏阔的背景。在它们暮色阴影的守护庇佑下，在此时节，远处的江水沉睡着，宛如一个神圣的婴孩，受到一位永恒乳母的守望。

我们挽着手臂沿这条道路前行，完全被眼前胜景的美所浸润折服，心意相通，默然前行。大约离城走了半英里时，毫无一丝征兆，大麻引发的剧烈战栗宛如晴天霹雳般击中了我。尽管平生此前仅体会过一次，其标志却如日常生活中最熟悉的事物一般确凿无疑。常有人请我解释这种战栗的本质，我也常常试图加以描摹，但世间没有任何类似之物能完美、乃至近似地传达它。与这种感受最为接近的，莫过于我们关于灵魂与肉体瞬间分离的概念。在尘世间，极少有人能在彻底死亡之前知晓伴随这种分离的状态，然而我们所有人对于其降临时必将呈现的模样，多多少少都有着某种清晰的概念。

===Original===

Even on this vague conception I throw myself for the sake of being understood with more confidence than I would dare to give to the most thorough description that I could elaborate.
The road along which we walked began slowly to lengthen. The hill over which it disappeared, at the distance of half a mile from me, soon came to be perceived as the boundary of the continent itself. But for the infinite loveliness of the sky, and waters, and fields, I should have been as greatly terrified with the increasing mystery of my state as I had been at the commencement of my first experience. But a most beautiful sunset was dying in the west, the river was tinged by it, the very zenith clouds were bathed in it, and the world beneath seemed to be floating in a dream of rosy tranquility. My awakened perceptions drank in this beauty until all sense of fear was banished, and every vein ran flooded with the very wine of delight. Mystery enwrapped me still, but it was the mystery of one who walks in Paradise for the first time.
Could I keep it from Dan? No, not for a moment. I had no remembrance of having taken hashish. The past was the property of another life, and I supposed that all the world was reveling in the same ecstasy as myself. I cast off all restraint; I leaped into the air; I clapped my hands, and shouted for joy. An involuntary exclamation raised the mustache of the poet beside me. “What in the world,” he cried, “is the matter with you?” I could only answer, “Bliss! bliss! unimagined bliss!” In an instant he saw all, for he knew my former experience, and as quickly formed the resolution of humoring me to the utmost in all my vagaries.
I glowed like a newborn soul. The well-known landscape lost all of its familiarity, and I was setting out upon a journey of years through heavenly territories, which it had been the longing of my previous lifetime to behold. “My dear friend,” I said, “we are about to realize all our youthful dreams of travel. Together you and I will wander on foot at our will through strange and beauteous countries; our life spreads before us henceforward unoccupied by cares, and the riches of all nature stretch onward through the immense domain we see in exultant expectancy to become the food for our thought and the fountains of our delight.

===Chinese===

为了能够被人理解，我宁可寄托于这一模糊的概念，也比胆敢给出我所能殚精竭虑推敲出的最详尽描述要自信得多。
我们走过的道路开始缓缓拉长。那座位于半英里外、道路没入其后的山峦，很快被我感知为整座大陆本身的边界。若非天空、水域与田野拥有无尽的娇媚，我本会因自身状态日盛的神秘而惊恐万状，恰如我初次体验的开端一样。然而，一抹最绚烂的落日正沉沦于西方，大江被它染红，连天顶的云彩亦沐浴其中，脚下的尘世宛如漂浮在一场玫瑰色宁谧的幻梦里。我觉醒的感知痛饮着这份至美，直到所有的恐惧感皆被驱散，每一根血管里都奔涌泛滥着愉悦的琼浆。神秘依然将我笼罩，但那是平生初次漫步在伊甸乐园之人的神秘。

我能对丹瞒得住吗？不，连一刻也瞒不住。我已毫无服食过大麻的记忆。过往属于另一段生命的财产，而我误以为全世界都在同我一样沉醉于相同的狂喜之中。我抛弃了一切拘束；我一跃腾空；我拍手欢呼，纵情雀跃。一声不由自主的惊呼，挑动了我身旁这位诗人的髭须。“你到底，”他喊道，“怎么回事啊？”我只能答道：“极乐！极乐！难以想象的极乐！”一瞬间他全明白了，因为他知晓我先前的经历，并立刻拿定主意，在我所有的荒唐怪诞中竭力顺从迎合我。

我如初生的灵魂般容光焕发。熟悉的景致尽数褪去了其凡俗的寻常面貌，我正踏上一场跨越数年的漫长旅途，穿行于天国疆域之中，那是我前生梦寐以求得见之境。“我亲爱的朋友，”我说，“我们即将实现青春年少时全部的漫游梦想。你我二人将携手徒步，随心所欲地穿行于奇异而壮丽的国度；从此往后，我们的人生铺展在前，再无忧烦羁绊，大自然的一切丰饶在我们眼前浩瀚无垠的疆域中向外铺展，满怀狂喜的期盼，化作我们思索的资粮与欢愉的源泉。

===Original===

To think that we should have been spared until this day﻿—spared to each other, spared for such glorious scenes! My friend, we shall travel together, linked soul to soul, and gaining ecstasy by impartition. At night, beneath the shade of zephyr-fanned mimosas, we shall lay ourselves down to sleep on the banks of primeval Asian rivers, and Bulbul shall sing us to sleep with his most delicious madrigals. When the first auroral tinges are glassed back from the peaks of Himalaya, we will arise, and, bathing ourselves in rock-o’ershadowed fountains, will start again upon our immortal way. Sleep shall repeat the echoes of the day to another and unfatigued inner sense of dreams, and awaking shall bear repetition of birth into newer and still more enchanting life. On! on!”
“I will go,” said my friend, “with delight.” Not a shadow of incredulousness or inappreciation passed over his face, and, drawing his arm still closer through my own, I hastened onward, as delighted with his consent as I was thoroughly convinced of the reality of the presence of grand old Asia.
The peculiar time of hashish, already so frequently mentioned, added one more rapturous element to my enjoyment. Through leagues of travel the shadows did not deepen around us, but the same unutterable sunset peace and beauty transfused the earth unchangeably. In watching the glories of the west at sunset in our ordinary state, they pass away from us so soon that the dying lustres have become to us almost the synonym for transition and decay. The golden masses become ruddy, the ruddy fall away to purple, the purple speedily grow black, and all this transmutation occupies no longer time than we may lean our foreheads, unfatigued, against a windowpane. In my present state of enlarged perception, Time had no kaleidoscope for me; nothing grew faint, nothing shifted, nothing changed except my ecstasy, which heightened through interminable degrees to behold the same rose-radiance lighting us up along all our immense journey. I might style my present chapter “Notes of Travel through the Champaigns of perpetual Sunset.”
From the road along which we traveled another leads back into P﻿—﻿—, across a more precipitous hill than any we had already ascended. Into this second road we turned.

===Chinese===

一想到我们竟能被眷顾留存至今日——彼此相守，得见如此恢弘壮景！我的朋友，我们将并肩同行，心心相印，在彼此的馈赠中得享狂喜。入夜时分，在和风轻拂的含羞草树荫下，我们将卧倒在太古亚洲河流的岸边安歇，夜莺（Bulbul）将用它最动人的小夜曲唱催我们入眠。当拂晓的初抹曙色自喜马拉雅群峰倒映而回时，我们将破晓起身，在岩石遮蔽的清泉中沐浴，重新踏上我们不朽的征途。沉睡将把白昼的回响重现给另一个不知疲倦的内在梦境感知，而苏醒则将一次次宣告我们在更新鲜、更迷人的生命中涅槃重生。前行！前行！”

“我愿同行，”我的朋友说，“欣喜之至。”他的脸上没有掠过一丝怀疑或不以为然的阴影；我将他的手臂挽得更紧，疾步向前，正如我坚信古老而宏伟的亚洲确实真实降临一样，因他的应允而欣喜若狂。

前文屡屡提及的大麻所特有的时间扭曲，为我的享乐增添了又一项令人陶醉的元素。在长达数里格的跋涉中，身周的暮色并未加深，同一种难以言喻的落日宁谧与至美，亘古不变地渗透着大地。在寻常状态下观赏西方落日的辉煌时，它们消逝得如此迅疾，以至于那垂死的余晖几乎成了流转与衰败的代名词。金黄色的云层化为酡红，酡红渐退为绛紫，绛紫迅速转为漆黑，而这一切的变幻沧桑，所耗费的时辰不过如我们不知疲倦地将额头倚在窗玻璃上的短短片刻。在我当下放大的感知状态中，时间对我而言不再是一只万花筒；没有事物变得黯淡，没有事物游移变换，除了我的狂喜——它经历着无休止的递增，目睹那同样的玫瑰色光辉在整场漫漫长途中照耀着我们。我大可将本章冠以《永恒落日旷野漫游札记》之名。

从我们行进的道路上，岔出另一条折返P市的道路，穿过一座比我们已登临过的任何山丘都要陡峭的岗峦。我们转入了这第二条道路。

===Original===

Yet, from the absence of all familiar appearances in the world around me, I did not suppose that we were returning to the town, but merely that we were continuing our journey through a new and less frequented bypath. Presently we struck a plank walk, and began mounting the hill of which I have spoken.
The moment that the planks began to resound beneath our feet I realized in what part of Asia we were journeying. We were on the great wall of China. Below us stretched into grand distances the plains of Tibet. Multitudinous were the flocks that covered them; countless groups of goats and goatherds were dispersed over the landscape as far as the eye could reach. The banks of innumerable streams were dotted with picturesque tents, and every minutest detail of the view in all respects harmonized with the idea of Asiatic life. Beyond Tibet, as with clairvoyant eyes, I looked straight through and over Hindu Kush, and beheld Cashmere sleeping in grand shadows. The fountains of the Punjab were unveiled, and among their spicy outflowings there gamboled, in Old-world freshness of heart, children of a primitive race whom prodigal nature had put beyond the necessity of labor. Through greenest valleys roved pairs of Oriental lovers, while above them flashed golden light from the fruit that hung in a Vallambrosa of citron-branches. Distance did not dim either scenery or countenances; every living thing was audible and visible in its rejoicing through leagues of light and shadow stretched between us. Again I leaped into the air and shouted for joy.
Along the road that skirted the outside of my Chinese wall a carriage came, drawn by a span of richly-caparisoned white horses. In it a young man and a maiden were sitting, and as they drew nearer they bowed to myself and my fellow-traveler. “Who are those?” asked Dan. “An eminent mandarin of the interior,” I replied, “of the order of the Blue Button, and by name Fuh-chieng, who, with his sister, at this season every year takes the tour of the provinces, dispensing justice and examining into the state of the public works. Verily, an estimable youth. Having known him during the summer we spent together at Peking, I feel constrained to speak with him.”

===Chinese===

然而，由于身周的世界全无半点熟悉的外貌，我并不以为我们正在返回市镇，而仅仅认为我们正沿着一条人迹罕至的全新僻径继续前行。不久，我们踏上了一条木板步道，开始攀登我所说的那座山岗。

脚下的木板一经发出声响，我便领悟到我们正漫游在亚洲的何方。我们正置身于万里长城之上。在我们下方，西藏（Tibet）的大平原延伸向宏阔的远方。平原上覆盖着密密麻麻的羊群；目力所及的整个画卷上，散布着数不清的山羊与牧羊人。无数溪流的岸边点缀着如画的帐篷，景致中每一个最微小的细节在各方面皆与亚洲生活的意象和谐契合。穿透西藏，宛如生有一双透视之眼，我直视并越过兴都库什山脉（Hindu Kush），望见克什米尔（Cashmere）正沉睡在壮丽的树荫阴影之中。旁遮普（Punjab）的泉眼敞开，在其芬芳四溢的流泉畔，原始种族的孩子们带着旧大陆的童真嬉戏雀跃，慷慨的大自然早已免除了他们劳作的辛劳。在至绿的山谷中，一对对东方的恋人漫步徜徉，头顶香橼树枝如瓦隆布罗萨（Vallambrosa）般繁密，倒挂的果实闪烁着金光。距离丝毫未能削弱景致或面庞的清晰；穿过横亘在我们之间的数里格光影，每一个活生生的生灵都在欢悦中清晰可闻、历历在目。我再次腾空跃起，纵情欢呼。

沿着环绕在我这道中国长城外侧的道路，驶来一辆由两匹披挂华丽马具的白马拉拽的马车。车内端坐着一位青年男子与一位少女，当他们走近时，向我和我的旅伴点头致意。“那是谁？”丹问道。“内地的一位显赫官员（mandarin），”我答道，“官居蓝顶，名叫福成（Fuh-chieng），每年此季他都会同胞妹巡视各省，断狱行赏，视察公工。当真是一位令人敬重的青年。我们在北京共度夏日时便已相识，我必须同他攀谈几句。”

===Original===

With a choice compliment on my lips, worded in the most courtly Chinese with which I as conversant, I was about to rush up the carriage and make my kowtow, when my friend, grasping my arm, entreated me to desist, begging to know whether I were not aware that, since the year 580 BC, when Ching-Chong was assassinated in his palanquin, it had been a criminal offense to approach within ten paces of a mandarin on his travels. “My dearest friend,” I replied, “you have saved me! I am astonished at your knowledge of Chinese law, this title of which had entirely escaped my mind. With thankfulness I yield to your suggestion, and will suffer the young man to pass on.” It was well that I did so, as my acquaintances in the carriage might otherwise have been terrified beyond measure by the singularity, if not by the sublimity of the dialect in which I should have addressed them.
It is possible for a man of imaginative mind, by mere suggestions of rich veins of thought, to lead a companion in the hashish state through visions of incomparable delight. This fact Dan had discovered in the good grace with which I instantly received his advice as to the mandarin. In our journeying we came to a tall gatepost of granite, which stood at the entrance to a lawn in front of one of the suburban residences of which I have spoken. Making his manner Oriental, to suit our supposed surroundings, he said to me, “Seest thou that tower that rises into the rosy air?” In an instant I beheld the tower with such conviction of reality that I did not even think of it as a metamorphose from something else. From the battlements flaunted yellow flags gorgeous with crimson dragons, and over each corner of the turret glared a rampant hippogriff, flaming, from his forked tongue even to his anomalous tail, with scales of dazzling gold. There was revelry within; its ecstasy worded in Semitic monosyllables, and accompanied by the mellifluous flights of gong and tom-tom. We passed on through Asia.
We now reached the summit of the hill. The broadest scope of vision which was possible was now ours. My ecstasy became so great that I seemed to cast off all shackles of flesh. The lover of beauty who should, for the first time, drink in the richness of this exalted view through the channels of the soul which are ordinarily opened, might well burst forth into singing were not reverence the stronger feeling.

===Chinese===

我唇边挂着精心挑选的赞词，措辞用上了我所通晓的最典雅的中国宫廷雅言，正打算冲上前去向马车行叩头大礼（kowtow），朋友却一把抓住我的胳膊，苦苦恳求我作罢，询问我是否不知道自公元前580年清昌（Ching-Chong）在轿中遇刺以来，靠近巡行官员十步之内便属于死罪犯科。“我最亲爱的朋友，”我答道，“你救了我一命！我对你通晓中国律法深感叹服，这一条款我全然忘到九霄云外去了。我心怀感激地采纳你的建议，任由这位青年走过去吧。”幸好我照办了，否则车上的熟人若非被我对他们说话时那方言的崇高感所震慑，也会被其荒谬怪诞吓得魂不附体。

富有想象力的人只需稍加点拨富于哲思的意绪，便能引导处于大麻状态下的同伴领略无可比拟的狂喜幻象。丹见我欣然采纳了他关于那位官员的规劝，便洞悉了这一诀窍。在我们的跋涉中，我们来到了一根高耸的花岗岩门柱前，它立在一处草坪入口，门后便是我提及的郊野宅邸之一。为了配合我们自以为身处的境遇，他摆出一副东方仪态对我说道：“你可曾看见那座耸入玫瑰色苍穹的高塔？”顷刻间，我看到了那座高塔，对其真实性深信不疑，甚至完全没意识到它是由旁物幻化而来的。城垛上飘扬着印有赤龙的绚烂黄旗，角楼的每个檐角都昂首怒视着一只跃立的骏鹰（hippogriff），从分叉的舌头直至畸形的尾巴，通体闪烁着耀目的金色鳞甲。塔内正举行着狂欢盛宴；其狂喜以闪米特单音节字词吐露，并伴随着铜锣与手鼓（tom-tom）悠扬回旋的齐鸣。我们继续在亚洲大陆上前行。

此时我们登上了山顶。目力所能企及的最为开阔的视野展现在我们眼前。我的狂喜是如此强烈，以至于我仿佛彻底挣脱了肉体的一切桎梏。凡初次借由常人敞开的灵魂渠道饱览这一崇高景致之丰饶的美之爱好者，若非敬畏之情更为强烈，恐怕早已情不自禁地引吭高歌了。

===Original===

But when, with me, that flow of loveliness broke in through doors in the spiritual nature to which no open sesame had ever before been granted, I felt, I cried out, “Why need we, in our journey, touch the earth at all? Let us sweep through air above this expanse of beauty, and read it like the birds.”
I was about to fly heavenward, chanting a triumphant hymn, when I turned and looked at Dan. He was standing sorrowfully, without means of flight. I was filled with contrition. “Dear brother of my pilgrimage,” I said, “did I speak of tempting the air, forgetful that thou wast not like unto myself? Forgive me﻿—I will not leave thee; yet, oh that thou couldst also fly! through what abysses of sublimity would we float!” Restoring myself to contentment with the airy tread of feet which hardly seemed to touch the ground, and my wish to oblivion, I again took his arm, and we voyaged as before.
Now we went singing, and I question whether Mozart ever rejoiced in his own musical creations as I did in that symphony we sang together. The tune and the words were extemporaneous, yet, by a close sympathy, he sang an accordant base to my air, and I heard delicious echoes thrown back from the dome of heaven. We sang the primal simplicity of Asia, the cradle of the nations, the grand expectancy of the younger continents, looking eastward to their mysterious mother for the gift of races still treasured in her womb. On our paean were borne the praises of the golden days of Foh and the serene prophecies of Confucius; we spoke of the rivers that for numberless centuries bore down to the eternal ocean no freight but the sere leaves of uninhabited wildernesses, whose shadows they glassed, and of fountains upon whose face no smile had rested save that of Hesper and the rising sun. I lived in what we sang: our music seemed a wondrous epic, whose pages we illustrated, not with pictures, but with living groups; the ancient days were restored before my eyes and to my ears, and I exulted in the perception with such conviction of reality that I ascribed it to no power of my own, but knew it as an exterior and universal fact.
This will be realized, perhaps, by very few who read my recital. The word for every strange phenomenon with all the world is “only imagination.”

===Chinese===

然而，当这份倾泻而入的至美冲开我灵性本质中此前从未获赐“芝麻开门”的大门时，我深切感触，高声呼喊道：“在我们的旅途中，何须踏足尘土半步？让我们凌空飞掠过这片无垠的美景，如飞鸟般饱览它的真容吧！”

我正打算吟唱着凯歌腾空飞向天际，猛一转头看向丹。他神情落寞地伫立着，全无飞行的手段。我心头涌起强烈的懊悔。“我朝圣之旅的亲爱兄弟啊，”我说，“难道我竟妄谈凌空翱翔，浑然忘却了你与我并非同一状态吗？原谅我吧——我绝不会弃你而去；然而，噢，若你亦能展翅飞翔该有多好！我们将漂浮穿行于何等崇高的深渊啊！”我重新满足于那双几乎不沾尘土的轻灵脚步，将翱翔的愿望抛诸脑后，重新挽起他的手臂，我们宛如先前一样继续远航。

此时我们一路引吭高歌，我甚至怀疑莫扎特在自己的音乐造物中所获得的欣喜，是否能比得上我从我们共同吟唱的交响乐中所汲取的快乐。曲调与词句皆是信手拈来的即兴之作，然而出于深契的心灵共鸣，他为我的旋律配上了和谐的低音，我听见天宇穹顶折射回甜美的回声。我们歌唱亚洲太古的质朴、万国的摇篮；歌唱年轻大洲的宏伟期盼——它们翘首东方，企盼着神秘的母亲赐予仍珍藏在其子宫之内的各族生灵。在我们的赞歌中，传颂着伏羲（Foh）黄金时代的赞美诗与孔子（Confucius）宁谧安详的预言；我们谈及那些流淌了无数世纪的大河，它们奔流入永恒的大洋，沿途除了所倒映的荒蛮旷野的枯叶之外未曾承载过任何货物；谈及那些除启明星（Hesper）与初升旭日之外未曾有过任何笑容拂面的清泉。我生活在我们所歌唱的意境之中：我们的音乐宛如一部奇妙的史诗，我们并非用图画、而是用栩栩如生的人物群像为其作注；远古岁月在我眼前与耳畔重焕生机，我为这一感知而欢欣雀跃，对其真实性坚信不疑，以至于我丝毫不将其归功于自己的力量，而是将其确信为外部客观的普世事实。

读到我这篇记述的人，或许极少能体会此情此景。世人面对一切离奇现象，脱口而出的无非都是“纯属想象”这四个字。

===Original===

Truly, this was imagination; but to me, with eyes and ears wide open in the daylight, an imagination as real as the soberest fact.
It will be remembered that the hashish states of ecstasy always alternate with less intense conditions, in which the prevailing phenomena are those of mirth or tranquility. In accordance with this law, in the present instance, Dan, to whom I had told my former experience, was not surprised to hear me break forth at the final cadence of our song into a peal of unextinguishable laughter, but begged to know what was its cause, that he might laugh too. I could only cry out that my right leg was a tin case filled with stair-rods, and as I limped along, keeping that member perfectly rigid, both from fear of cracking the metal and the difficulty of bending it, I heard the rattle of the brazen contents shaken from side to side with feelings of the most supreme absurdity possible to the human soul. Presently the leg was restored to its former state, but in the interim its mate had grown to a size which would have made it a very respectable trotter for Brian Boru or one of the Titans. Elevated some few hundred feet into the firmament, I was compelled to hop upon my giant pedestal in a way very ungraceful in a world where two legs were the fashion, and eminently disagreeable to the slighted member, which sought in vain to reach the earth with struggles amusing from their very insignificance. This ludicrous affliction being gradually removed, I went on my way quietly until we again began to be surrounded by the houses of the town.
Here the phenomenon of the dual existence once more presented itself. One part of me awoke, while the other continued in perfect hallucination. The awakened portion felt the necessity of keeping in side streets on the way home, lest some untimely burst of ecstasy should startle more frequented thoroughfares. I mentioned this to Dan, who drew me into a quiet lane, by the side of which we sat down together to rest on a broad stone. By this time the sunset had nearly faded, while my attention was directed to other things, and its regency of all the beauties of the sky was replaced by that of the full moon, now at the zenith. A broad and clearly-defined halo surrounded her, and refracted her rays in such a manner as to shower them from its edge in a prismatic fringe.

===Chinese===

诚然，这确是想象；但对于光天化日之下耳聪目明的我而言，这份想象却同最严谨的事实一样真切不虚。

读者当会忆起，大麻的狂喜状态总会与较为平缓的状态交替出现，在后者中，占据主导地位的现象是欢愉或宁静。依循这一法则，在当下的情境中，由于我曾向丹讲述过先前的经历，他在我们歌声落幕的尾音中听到我突然爆发出一阵止不住的狂笑时，并未感到丝毫惊诧，只是央求我说明缘由，好让他也跟着开怀大笑。我只能大喊说我的右腿成了一只塞满楼梯铜压条（stair-rods）的马口铁桶；当我一瘸一拐地走着时，由于既担心金属碎裂又难以弯折，我将这条腿绷得笔直，听着里面的黄铜家什随着身躯晃动左右碰撞作响，心中涌起人类灵魂所能体会到的登峰造极的荒诞感。不多时，右腿恢复了原状，然而在此期间，它的另一半伙伴却暴长至足以担当布赖恩·博鲁（Brian Boru）或泰坦巨人一条体面健足的庞大尺寸。由于被高高抬升至数百英尺的高空之中，在这个崇尚双腿步行的尘世上，我不得不以极其不雅的姿势倚着这根巨大的基座单腿跳跃，这让那条备受冷落的短腿极其懊丧，它徒劳地挣扎着想要触碰地面，其无足轻重的扑腾反而显得尤为滑稽。当这出滑稽的折磨逐渐消解后，我平静地继续前行，直到我们四周再次被城镇的房舍所环绕。

在此处，双重存在的现象再次展现。我的一部分苏醒过来，而另一部分则继续沉浸在完全的幻觉之中。清醒的部分意识到在归途中必须走僻静小巷，以免突如其来的狂喜爆发惊吓到人流更稠密的大道。我向丹提到了这一点，他便将我引如一条幽静的小巷，我们在巷旁的一块宽大石块上并肩坐下歇息。此时，正当我的注意力被旁的事物牵引之际，落日已几乎褪尽，它对天际万千美景的统治已被此刻高悬中天的满月所取代。一圈宽阔而轮廓分明的月晕环绕着她，并以一种从其边缘洒下棱镜边缘虹彩流光的方式，折射着她的清辉。

===Original===

That vision of loveliness was the only possible one which could have recompensed me for the loss of my sunset. I gazed heavenward, as one fascinated by mystical eyes. And now the broad luminous belt began to be peopled with myriads of shining ones from the realm of Faëry who plunged into the translucent lake of ether as into a sea, and dashing back its silvery spray from their breasts, swam to the moon and ascended its gleaming beach.
Between this moon-island and the shore of halo now growing multitudes endlessly passed and repassed, and I could hear, tinkling down through the vacant spaces, the thrill of their gnome-laughter. I could have kept that stony seat all night, and looked speechlessly into heaven, unmoved though an armed host had passed by me on the earth, but unconsciously I closed my eyes, and was in a moment whirling on through a visionary dance, like that in which I had been borne as soon as I lay down at the time of my first experiment. Temples and gardens, pyramids and unearthly rivers, began to float along before the windows of my sense, when Dan, looking around, saw that I would become unconscious, and aroused me. Again we walked on.
And now that unutterable thirst which characterizes hashish came upon me. I could have lain me down and lapped dew from the grass. I must drink, wheresoever, howsoever. We soon reached home﻿—soon, because it was not five squares off from where we sat down, yet ages, from the thirst which consumed me and the expansion of time in which I lived. I came into the house as one would approach a fountain in the desert, with a wild bound of exultation, and gazed with miserly eyes at the draught which my friend poured out for me until the glass was brimming. I clutched it﻿—I put it to my lips. Ha! a surprise! It was not water, but the most delicious metheglin in which ever bard of the Cymri drank the health of Howell Dda. It danced and sparkled like some liquid metempsychosis of amber; it gleamed with the spiritual fire of a thousand chrysolites. To sight, to taste it was metheglin, such as never mantled in the cups of Valhalla.
The remainder of the evening I spent in a delirium which, unlike all that had preceded it, was one of unutterable calm.

===Chinese===

唯有这般绝美的景象，才足以补偿我失去落日的遗憾。我仰望苍穹，宛如被一双神秘的眼眸所蛊惑。此刻，那道宽广明亮的光带上开始汇聚起无数来自仙境（Faëry）的光辉生灵，它们纵身跃入清澈的以太之湖，宛如跳入大海一般，胸膛破开银色的浪花，向着月亮游去，并登上了她那熠熠生辉的沙滩。

在这座月亮之岛与月晕边缘的海岸之间，日益庞大的人群络绎不绝地往返穿梭，我能听见穿透虚空飘落而下的阵阵小精灵般的清脆笑声。我本可以在那块石座上枯坐通宵，默默凝视天穹，即便一支全副武装的军队从我身旁的尘世踏步而过也绝不动容；然而我无意识地阖上了双眼，顷刻间在一场幻象的狂舞中旋转飞驰，恰如初次实验时我刚躺下便被卷入的狂澜一样。神殿与园林、金字塔与超凡脱俗的河流开始在我感官的明窗前漂浮而过，此时丹环顾四周，见我即将陷入昏迷，便将我唤醒。我们再次迈步前行。

此时，大麻所特有的那种难以言表的干渴席卷了我。我恨不得直接扑倒在地，舔舐草叶上的甘露。无论在何处、用何种方式，我都必须喝水。我们很快到家了——说快，是因为离我们坐下的地方不过五个街区之遥；说漫长如漫漫岁月，则是因为那折磨着我的焦渴以及我所置身的时间膨胀。我冲进家门，宛如跋涉在沙漠中的旅人扑向泉眼，带着狂喜的跃动，以守财奴般的贪婪目光紧盯着朋友为我倒满的一杯水，直到杯沿盈满。我一把抓过水杯——凑到唇边。哈！真是意外！那根本不是水，而是威尔士吟游诗人为豪厄尔·达（Howell Dda）祝酒时饮过的最醇美的蜂蜜酒（metheglin）。它宛如琥珀化作的流体转世般跳跃闪耀；它闪烁着上千颗金绿宝石的空灵火焰。无论观其色还是辨其味，它都是蜂蜜琼浆，连瓦尔哈拉（Valhalla）神殿的杯盏中也未曾泛起过如此玉液。

那个夜晚余下的时光，我沉浸在一场谵妄之中；然而与先前经历的一切截然不同，这一场谵妄散发着难以言说的宁谧。

===Original===

Not the heavy sleep of a debauch, not the voluntary musing of the visionary, but a clarifying of all thought, and the lowing in of the richest influences from the world around me, without the toil of selecting them. I looked at the stars, and felt kindred with them; I spoke to them, and they answered me. I dwelt in an inner communion with heaven﻿—a communion where every language is understood, rather where all speak the same language, and deeply did I realize a voice which seemed to say, as in my waking dreams I had faintly heard in murmur upon earth,

> πολλαὶ μὲν θνητοῖς γλῶτται, μία δ’ἀθανάτοισιν

===Chinese===

那并非放纵沉湎后的沉滞酣睡，亦非幻想家蓄意的冥思苦想，而是所有思绪的澄澈澄清，以及身周世界最丰饶的美妙感召无须费心甄选便涌入心头。我仰望繁星，感到与它们息息相通；我同它们倾谈，它们向我应答。我栖居在与天国的心灵交融之中——在那交融中，每一种语言皆被领会，或者说，所有生灵皆操着同一种语言；我深切领悟到一个声音，仿佛正如我在清醒的梦境中曾在凡尘隐约听闻的呢喃：

> “凡人操千言，仙众共一语。”

## V The Hour and the Power of Darkness / 第五章 黑暗时刻与黑暗权柄
===Original===

It may perhaps be not altogether a fanciful classification to divide every man’s life into two periods, the locomotive and the static. Restless fluidity always characterizes the childish mind in its healthy state, exemplifying itself in the thousand wayward freaks, hair-breadth experiments, and unanswerable questions which keep the elder portions of a family in continual oscillation between mirth and terror. There is not always a thorough solidification of the mental nature, even when the great boy has learned what to do with his hands, and how to occupy his station at maturer tea-parties with becoming dignity and resignation. No longer, to be sure, does he gratify experimental tendencies by taking the eight-day clock to pieces to look at its machinery; no longer does he nonplus grave aunts and grandmothers with questions upon the causes of his own origination, but the same dynamic propensities exist expanded into a larger and more self-conscious sphere. His restlessness of limb has now become the desire of travel, his investigation into the petty matters of household economy has grown into a thirst for research whose field is the world and whose instruments are the highest faculties of induction.
With some men this state remains unchanged through a long life, but to most of us there comes, sooner or later, a period when the longing for change dies out, and a fixed place and an unalterable condition become the great central ideas of existence. We look back with a wonder that is almost incredulousness upon the time when a ride by railway was the dream of weeks preceding, and try in vain to realize the supernatural freshness which the earth put on when for the first time we discovered that we were nearsighted, and looked through some friend’s spectacles. Motion, except for the rare purpose of recreation, becomes an annoyance to us beyond a circumscribed territory, and we have emerged into the static condition of life before we are aware.
Much earlier than the usual period did this become the case with me. A feeble childhood soon exhausted its superfluous activities, and into books, ill health, and musing I settled down when I should have been playing cricket, hunting, or riding.

===Chinese===

将每个人的人生划分为两个时期——运动期与静止期——或许绝非异想天开的分类。处于健康状态下的童年心智，始终以躁动不安的流动性为特征，体现为成千上万任性的恶作剧、险象环生的实验以及令人哑口无言的诘问，让家里的长辈们始终在欢笑与惊骇之间摇摆不定。即便当半大小子学会了安放双手，并在长辈的茶会上以应有的体面与顺从安守本分时，心智的本质也未必总能彻底定型固化。诚然，他不再会借由拆卸八天发条钟以窥探其内部机械构造来满足实验冲动；他不再会用追问自身起源的问题令严肃的姑母与祖母们瞠目结舌；然而，同样的动能倾向依然存在，并膨胀为一个更宏大、更具自觉意识的领域。他四肢的躁动不安如今化作了周游世界的渴望；他对治家琐事的探究，已演变为对研究的渴求——其涉足的疆域是整个世界，其运用的工具是人类最高超的归纳机能。

在某些人身上，这种状态终其一生未曾改变；但对我们大多数人而言，迟早会迎来这样一个阶段：求变的渴望逐渐熄灭，固定的居所与一成不变的境遇成了生存的核心理念。我们回首往昔——在那个年代，乘火车出行曾是提前数周就魂牵梦萦的夙愿；当我们平生初次发现自己近视并透过朋友的眼镜凝视尘世时，大地所披覆的那层超自然的新奇清新，我们如今再怎么努力也难以重现——带着近乎难以置信的惊异重温旧梦。除却罕有的消遣目的之外，一旦跨出有限的领地，活动便成了我们的累赘，而在我们浑然不觉之中，我们已然步入了人生的静止状态。

于我而言，这种状态的到来远比寻常时期早得多。孱弱的童年很快耗尽了我过剩的精力；当同龄人本该击打板球、狩猎或驰骋马背时，我却早已沉溺于书卷、疾病与玄思遐想之中。

===Original===

The younger thirst for adventure was quenched by rapid degrees as I found it possible to ascend Chimborazo with Humboldt lying on a sofa, or chase hartebeests with Cumming over muffins and coffee. The only exceptions to this state of imaginative indolence were the hours spent in rowing or sailing upon the most glorious river of the world, and the consciousness that the Hudson rolled at my own door only contributed to settle the conviction that there was no need of going abroad to find beauties in which the soul might wrap itself as in a garment of delight. Even at these seasons exercise was not so much the aim as musing. Many a time, with the handles of my sculls thrust under the side-girders, and the blades turned full to the wind, have I sat and drifted for hours through mountain-shadows, and past glimpses of light that flooded the woody gorges, with a sense of dreamy ecstasy which all the novelties of a new world could never have supplied.
Oh, most noble river, what hast thou not been to me? In childhood thy ripples were the playmates of my perpetual leisure, dancing up the sandy stretches of thy brink, and telling laughing tales of life’s beamy spray and sunshine. In after years, the grand prophet of a wider life, thine ebb sang chants to the imperial ocean, into whose pearly palaces thou wast hastening, and thy flood brought up the resounding history of the infinite surges whence thou hadst returned. It is not thine to come stealing from unnamed fountains of mystery, nor to crown thy sublime mountains with the ruined battlements of a departed age; but more than Nile hath. God glorified thee, and Nature hath hallowed thy walls with her own armorial bearings till thou art more reverend than Rhine. On thy guarding peaks Antiquity sits enthroned, asking no register in the crumbling monuments of man, but bearing her original sceptre from the hand of Him who first founded her domain beside thy immortal flow.
Gradually the Hudson came to supply all my spiritual wants. Were I sad, I found sympathy in the almost human murmurs of his waters, as, stretched upon the edge of some rocky headland, I heard them go beating into the narrow caves beneath me, and return sighing, as if defrauded of a hiding-place and a home. Were I merry, the whitecaps danced and laughed about my prancing boat, and the wind whistled rollicking glees against my stays.

===Chinese===

昔日对冒险的渴求迅速熄灭，因为我发觉自己完全可以躺在沙发上跟随洪堡（Humboldt）攀登钦博拉索山（Chimborazo），或是一边品着松饼与咖啡，一边跟随卡明（Cumming）追逐大羚羊。这种想象力的慵懒状态唯一的例外，便是在世间最壮丽的大河上划艇或扬帆泛舟的时光；而深知哈德逊河就在自家门前奔流，更坚定了我的一项笃信：无须远涉重洋去追寻能让灵魂如披华服般裹入喜悦之中的至美。即便在这些时节，强身健体也非主要目的，冥想才是真谛。不知有多少次，我将双桨的握把卡在舷侧梁下，桨叶迎风平展，静静坐着，在山影中漂流数个小时，掠过淹没树木繁茂峡谷的浮光掠影，心中充盈着梦幻般的狂喜，那是新大陆的一切新奇事物皆无法赋予的。

噢，至为崇高的大河，于我而言你何曾不是一切？在童年时代，你的涟漪是我永恒闲暇的玩伴，在岸边沙滩上欢跃起舞，欢笑着诉说着生命光彩照人的浪花与艳阳的故事。在及长之后的岁月中，作为更辽阔生命的伟大先知，你的落潮向帝王般的浩瀚大洋吟唱赞歌，向着大洋的珍珠宫殿奔流疾驰；而你的涨潮，则带来了你由之归来的无垠浪涛那铿锵轰鸣的历史。你无需从无名神秘的泉眼中潜行而出，亦无需用逝去时代的残破城堞来为你崇高的山峦加冕；然而上帝荣耀你更甚于尼罗河，大自然用她自己的纹章圣化了你的岩壁，令你比莱茵河更加令人肃然起敬。在你守护的山巅之上，远古高踞王座，无需在人类风化坍塌的纪念碑上留下记录，而是从那位最初在你永恒奔流之畔奠定其疆域的造物主手中，承接她原始的权杖。

渐渐地，哈德逊河满足了我全部的心灵需求。若我悲伤，便能在江水那近乎人类般的低语中寻得慰藉；当我仰卧在某处岩石岬角的边缘，听着它们拍击着我下方的逼仄岩洞，随后叹息着退回，宛如被剥夺了藏身之所与家园。若我欢快，白色的浪花便在我跃动的小舟旁欢舞欢笑，风儿吹拂着我的桅索，呼啸着奏响狂放欢愉的齐唱曲。

===Original===

In weariness, I leaped into the stream; his cool hand upbore and caressed me till I returned braced for thought, and renewed as by a plunge into El Dorado. In the Hudson I found a wealth which satisfied all wishes, and my supreme hope was that on his banks I might pass all my life. Thus supplied with beauty, consolation, dreams, all things, every day I became more and more careless of the world beyond, and in my frame grew even *hyperstatic*.
It was in this state that hashish found me. After the walk which I last recorded, the former passion for travel returned with powerful intensity. I had now a way of gratifying it which comported both with indolence and economy. The whole East, from Greece to farthest China, lay within the compass of a township; no outlay was necessary for the journey. For the humble sum of six cents I might purchase an excursion ticket over all the earth; ships and dromedaries, tents and hospices were all contained in a box of Tilden’s extract. Hashish I called the “drug of travel,” and I had only to direct my thoughts strongly toward a particular part of the world previously to swallowing my bolus to make my whole fantasia in the strongest possible degree topographical. Or, when the delirium was at its height, let anyone suggest to me, however faintly, mountain, wilderness, or marketplace, and straightway I was in it, drinking in the novelty of my surroundings in all the ecstasy of a discoverer. I swam up against the current of all time; I walked through Luxor and Palmyra as they were of old; on Babylon the bittern had not built her nest, and I gazed on the unbroken columns of the Parthenon.
Soon after my pedestrian journey through Asia I changed my residence for a while, and went to live in the town of Schenectady. It was here that the remainder of my hashish-life was passed, and here, for many days, did I drain alternately cups of superhuman joy and superhuman misery. At Union College, of which I was a resident, I had a few friends to whom I communicated my acquaintance with the wondrous drug which was now becoming a habit with me.

===Chinese===

倦怠之时，我便跃入水中；清凉的大手托举抚慰着我，直到我归来时重新精神抖擞，思绪敏捷，宛如纵身跳入黄金国（El Dorado）般重获新生。在哈德逊河中，我寻得了足以满足所有祈愿的财富，我至高无上的夙愿便是在这江畔度尽余生。如此被美、慰藉、梦境以及万物所滋养，我日益对身外的凡尘漠不关心，体质甚至变得*极度静止*。

正是在这种状态下，大麻与我不期而遇。在上次记录的那场漫步之后，昔日周游的狂热以强大的烈度卷土重来。我如今有了一种既符合慵懒又兼顾节俭的满足方式。从希腊到极远之中国，整个东方皆被纳入了一座乡镇的方圆之内；这场旅行无须任何花费。只需区区六美分（six cents）的微薄代价，我便能购得一张环游整个地球的漫游车票；轮船与单峰骆驼、帐篷与收容驿站，皆包含在一盒蒂尔登浸膏之中。我将大麻称作“旅行之药”，在吞下药丸之前，我只需将思绪强力导向世界的某一特定角落，便能让我整幅幻境画卷展现出最强烈的地理地貌特征。或者，当谵妄达到顶峰时，任凭任何人向我哪怕最微弱地提及山峦、荒野或集市，我便立刻身临其境，以探索者的全部狂喜痛饮周遭环境的新奇。我逆着万古岁月的洪流逆流而上；我漫步穿过昔日的卢克索（Luxor）与巴尔米拉（Palmyra）；在巴比伦，麻鳽尚未筑巢，我凝视着帕特农神庙完好无损的列柱。

在徒步穿行亚洲的旅程后不久，我换了一处暂居地，搬到了斯克内克塔迪镇（Schenectady）居住。正是在这里，我度过了余下的大麻岁月；正是在这里，日复一日，我交替饮尽了超凡的极乐与超凡的苦难之杯。在我就读留宿的联合学院（Union College），有几位朋友得知了我结识这种奇药的秘密，而它如今正逐渐变成我的癖好。

===Original===

Some of them were surprised, some warned me, and as they will most of them be introduced into the narrative which I am writing, I now mention them thus particularly, lest it may be thought strange that, in an ordinary town of small size, there should be found by one man a sufficient number of congenial persons to vary the dramatis personae of a story as mine will be varied.
Having exhausted the supply of hashish which I had originally obtained from the shelves of my old lounging-place at the shop of the doctor, I procured a small jar of a preparation of the same drug by another chemist, which, I was told, was much weaker than the former. Late in the evening I took about fifty grains of the new preparation, arguing that this amount was a rational equivalent for the thirty which had before been my maximum dose.
It is impossible, however, to base any calculation of the energy of hashish upon such a comparison. The vital forces upon which this most magical stimulant operates are too delicate, too recondite to be treated like material parts in a piece of mechanism whose power of resistance can be definitely expressed by an equation. There are certain nerves, no doubt, which the anatomist and the physician will find affected by the cannabine influence﻿—certain functions over which its essence appears to hold peculiar regency; but we must have proceeded much farther in the science which treats of the connection between matter and mind, must know much more of those imponderable forces which, more delicate than electricity and more mysterious than the magnetic fluid, weave the delicate interacting network that joins our human duality, before we can treat that part of us affected by hashish as a constant in any calculation.
There are two facts which I have verified as universal by repeated experiment, which fall into their place here as aptly as they can in the course of my narrative: 1st: At two different times, when body and mind are apparently in precisely analogous states, when all circumstances, exterior and interior, do not differ tangibly in the smallest respect, the same dose of the same preparation of hashish will frequently produce diametrically opposite effects.

===Chinese===

他们之中有的感到诧异，有的警告我；鉴于他们大多数人都将在我笔下的叙事中登场，我如今特此提及他们，以免读者觉得奇怪：在这样一座寻常的小城镇里，一个人怎能找到足够多的意气相投之人，来丰富我故事中即将展现的那般形形色色的登场人物。

在耗尽了最初从我常去的消遣之所——医生的药铺搁架上取得的大麻储备之后，我从另一位化学家那里买来了一小罐同种药物的制剂，据说药效远弱于前者。夜深时分，我服用了大约五十格令（fifty grains）的新制剂，心想这个分量理应合理等同于我先前的最大剂量三十格令。

然而，试图基于这种比照来测算大麻的效力是绝无可能的。这种最神奇的兴奋剂所作用的生命机能过于微妙深奥，绝不能将其视作机械装置中可用方程式精确表达抗力的物质构件。毫无疑问，解剖学家与医生会发现某些神经受到了大麻素的影响——其精华似乎对某些机能拥有奇特的统摄力；但在探究物质与心灵关联的科学取得深远进展之前，在我们对那些比电流更精微、比磁流更神秘、编织出连接人类双重性微妙相互交织之网络的无从称量之力有更多了解之前，我们绝无法在任何计算中将自身受大麻影响的部位视作常数。

有两桩事实已被我通过反复实验证实具有普遍性，在此恰可顺理成章地纳入叙事之中：其一，在两个不同的时间点，即便身心显然处于完全类似的状态，内外一切境况在最微小的细节上皆无实质差异，同一种大麻制剂的同一剂量，却常常会产生截然相反的效果。

===Original===

Still further, I have taken at one time a pill of thirty grains, which hardly gave a perceptible phenomenon, and at another, when my dose had been but half that quantity, I have suffered the agonies of a martyr, or rejoiced in a perfect frenzy. So exceedingly variable are its results, that, long before I abandoned the indulgence, I took each successive bolus with the consciousness that I was daring an uncertainty as tremendous as the equipoise between hell and heaven. Yet the fascination employed Hope as its advocate, and won the suit. 2nd: If, during the ecstasy of hashish delirium, another dose, however small﻿—yes, though it be no larger than half a pea﻿—be employed to prolong the condition, such agony will inevitably ensue as will make the soul shudder at its own possibility of endurance without annihilation. By repeated experiments, which now occupy the most horrible place upon my catalogue of horrible remembrances, have I proved that, among all the variable phenomena of hashish, this alone stands unvarying. The use of it directly after any other stimulus will produce consequences as appalling.
But to return from my digression. It was perhaps eight o’clock in the evening when I took the dose of fifty grains. I did not retire until near midnight, and as no effects had then manifested themselves, I supposed that the preparation was even weaker than my ratio gave it credit for being, and, without any expectation of result, lay down to sleep. Previously, however, I extinguished my light. To say this may seem trivial, but it is as important a matter as any which it is possible to notice. The most direful suggestions of the bottomless pit may flow in upon the hashish-eater through the very medium of darkness. The blowing out of a candle can set an unfathomed barathrum wide agape beneath the flower-wreathed table of his feast, and convert his palace of sorcery into a Golgotha. Light is a necessity to him, even when sleeping; it must tinge his visions, or they assume a hue as sombre as the banks of Styx.
I do not know how long a time had passed since midnight, when I awoke suddenly to find myself in a realm of the most perfect clarity of view, yet terrible with an infinitude of demoniac shadows. Perhaps, I thought, I am still dreaming; but no effort could arouse me from my vision, and I realized that I was wide awake.

===Chinese===

更有甚者，我曾有一次服下一粒三十格令的药丸，却几乎未见任何明显反应；而在另一次，当剂量仅有前者的一半时，我却遭受了殉道者般的剧痛，或是陷入完全的狂喜狂热。其结果变幻莫测到了这般地步，以至于早在戒绝这种沉湎之前，我每次服下大药丸时都清醒地意识到：自己正冒着堪比地狱与天堂之平衡般巨大的未知风险。然而，那份魔力聘请了“希望”作为它的辩护人，并打赢了官司。其二，若在大麻谵妄的狂喜之中，再补服另一剂药——无论多么微小，是的，哪怕不及半粒豌豆大——以期延长药效，必然会引发极度的痛苦，令灵魂对其在未曾湮灭的情况下竟然能够承受如此苦难而毛骨悚然。通过反复的实验——它们如今在我恐怖记忆的册卷中占据着最惊悚的篇章——我证实了在大麻所有千变万化的现象中，唯独这一条恒定不移。在任何其他刺激物之后立即服用大麻，亦将产生同样骇人的后果。

言归正传。服下那剂五十格令的药丸时，大约是晚上八点。直到近午夜我才就寝，由于当时尚未显现任何药效，我便以为该药剂甚至比我预估的效力还要微弱，于是在不抱任何反应指望的情况下卧床入眠。然而在入睡之前，我吹灭了灯火。提及此事看似微不足道，却是所能留意到的最关键的事项。无底深渊最凶险的暗示，正可能借由黑暗这一媒介涌入食大麻者的心头。吹熄蜡烛，便能在他鲜花簇拥的盛宴餐桌下方撕开一道深不可测的裂口深渊（barathrum），将他的巫术宫殿化作各各他（Golgotha）骷髅地。光亮对他而言乃是不可或缺之物，哪怕在睡眠中亦是如此；它必须为他的幻象染上色彩，否则那些景象便会呈现出如冥河斯提克斯（Styx）河岸般阴森凄厉的色调。

不知自午夜之后流逝了多久，我猛然惊醒，发觉自己置身于一个视野极致澄澈清明、却因无尽魔影而恐怖万分的领域。或许，我想，自己仍在梦中；然而无论怎样挣扎都无法从幻象中苏醒，我意识到自己已完全清醒。

===Original===

Yet it was an awakening which, for torture, had no parallel in all the stupendous domain of sleeping incubus. Beside my bed in the centre of the room stood a bier, from whose corners drooped the folds of a heavy pall; outstretched upon it lay in state a most fearful corpse, whose livid face was distorted with the pangs of assassination. The traces of a great agony were frozen into fixedness in the tense position of every muscle, and the nails of the dead man’s fingers pierced his palms with the desperate clinch of one who has yielded not without agonizing resistance. Two tapers at his head, two at his feet, with their tall and unsnuffed wicks, made the ghastliness of the bier more luminously unearthly, and a smothered laugh of derision from some invisible watcher ever and anon mocked the corpse, as if triumphant demons were exulting over their prey. I pressed my hands upon my eyeballs till they ached, in intensity of desire to shut out the spectacle; I buried my head in the pillow, that I might not hear that awful laugh of diabolic sarcasm.
But﻿—oh horror immeasurable! I beheld the walls of the room slowly gliding together, the ceiling coming down, the floor ascending, as of the lonely captive saw them, whose cell was doomed to be his coffin. Nearer and nearer am I borne toward the corpse. I shrunk back from the edge of the bed; I cowered in most abject fear. I tried to cry out, but speech was paralyzed. The walls came closer and closer together. Presently my hand lay on the dead man’s forehead. I made my arm as straight and rigid as a bar of iron; but of what avail was human strength against the contraction of that cruel masonry? Slowly my elbow bent with the ponderous pressure; nearer grew the ceiling﻿—I fell into the fearful embrace of death. I was pent, I was stifled in the breathless niche, which was all of space still left to me. The stony eyes stared up into my own, and again the maddening peal of fiendish laughter rang close beside my ear. Now I was touched on all sides by the walls of the terrible press; there came a heavy crush, and I felt all sense blotted out in darkness.
I awaked at last; the corpse was gone, but I had taken his place upon the bier. In the same attitude which he had kept I lay motionless, conscious, although in darkness, that I wore upon my face the counterpart of his look of agony.

===Chinese===

然而，就折磨而言，这种清醒在沉睡梦魇（incubus）的整个庞大领域中绝无匹敌。在房间中央我的床榻旁，立着一副灵柩台，厚重棺罩的褶皱从四角垂落；一具极其可怖的尸体陈列其上，青灰的面庞因遇刺惨死的剧痛而扭曲变形。极度痛苦的痕迹在每一块肌肉的紧绷中凝固不动，死者双手的指甲深深刺入掌心，带着拼死抵抗未果之人的绝望抓握。头顶两侧各点一根蜡烛，脚边亦立两根，长长未剪的烛芯让灵柩台的阴森可怖显得更加非人般通明；而不时从某位看不见的窥视者喉中传出的压抑嘲笑声，肆意讥弄着尸体，宛如凯旋的恶魔正对其猎物狂喜庆贺。我用双手死死按住眼球，直到按得生疼，竭力想要隔绝这幕惨状；我将头深埋进枕头里，只求听不见那魔鬼般讽刺的恐怖笑声。

然而——噢，无边的恐怖！我看见房间的四壁正缓慢地靠拢滑动，天花板下沉，地板上升，恰如那注定以囚室为棺椁的孤绝囚徒所目睹的那样。我离那具尸体被推挤得越来越近。我从床沿向后蜷缩；在极度卑微的恐惧中瑟瑟发抖。我试图叫喊，但咽喉麻痹无法发声。四壁越聚越拢。不多时，我的手已搭在死者的额头上。我将手臂绷得像铁棍一样笔直僵硬；但在那残酷砖石结构的向内挤压面前，凡人的力量又有何用？在沉重的挤压下，我的肘部慢慢弯曲；天花板愈发逼近——我跌入了死神可怖的怀抱之中。我被禁锢、被窒息在残存的狭窄窒息壁龛里，那是留给我的全部空间。死者僵死的双眼向上死死盯住我的双眸，魔鬼般令人发狂的阵阵刺耳笑声再次贴着我的耳畔回荡。此刻，四周可怕挤压的墙壁已触及我的全身；一阵沉重的挤压声传来，我感到所有知觉皆在黑暗中被彻底抹去。

我终于醒了过来；尸体不见了，但我却取代了他的位置躺在灵柩台上。我保持着他先前的姿态一动不动地躺着，尽管身处黑暗之中，我却清楚地意识到自己的脸上正浮现着同他如出一辙的痛苦神情。

===Original===

The room had grown into a gigantic hall, whose roof was framed of iron arches; the pavement, the walls, the cornice were all of iron. The spiritual essence of the metal seemed to be a combination of cruelty and despair. It’s massive hardness spoke a language which it is impossible to embody in words, but anyone who has watched the relentless sweep of some great engine crank, and realized its capacity for murder, will catch a glimpse, even in the memory, of the thrill which seemed to say, “This iron is a tearless fiend,” of the unutterable meaning I saw in those colossal beams and buttresses. I suffered from the vision of that iron as from the presence of a giant assassin.
But my senses opened slowly to the perception of still worse presences. By my side there gradually emerged from the sulphureous twilight which bathed the room the most horrible form which the soul could look upon unshattered﻿—a fiend also of iron, white hot and dazzling with the glory of the nether penetralia. A face that was the ferreous incarnation of all imaginations of malice and irony looked on me with a glare, withering from its intense heat, but still more from the unconceived degree of inner wickedness which it symbolized. I realized whose laughter I had heard, and instantly I heard it again. Beside him another demon, his very twin, was rocking a tremendous cradle framed of bars of iron like all things else, and candescent with as fierce a heat as the fiend’s.
And now, in a chant of the most terrific blasphemy which it is possible to imagine, or rather of blasphemy so fearful that no human thought has ever conceived of it, both the demons broke forth, until I grew intensely wicked merely by hearing it. I still remember the meaning of the song, although there is no language yet coined which will convey it, and far be it from me even to suggest its nature, lest I should seem to perpetuate in any degree such profanity as beyond the abodes of the lost no lips are capable of uttering. Every note of the music itself accorded with the thought as symbol represents essence, and with its clangor mixed the maddening creak of the forever-oscillating cradle, until I felt driven into a ferocious despair. Suddenly the nearest fiend, snatching up a pitchfork (also of white-hot iron), thrust it into my writhing side, and hurled me shrieking into the fiery cradle.

===Chinese===

房间已膨胀为一座巨大的钢铁大厅，屋顶由一道道铁拱构成；地面、墙壁、檐口皆为纯铁打造。这种金属的精神实质似乎正是残忍与绝望的交织体。其厚重的坚硬诉说着一种言语无法尽述的语言；但凡见过某台巨型蒸汽机曲柄无情扫荡并领悟到其杀戮威力的人，哪怕仅在记忆中，亦能捕捉到那一丝仿佛在说“这钢铁是不知垂泪的恶魔”的震颤，体会到我在那些庞大铁梁与扶壁中窥见的难以言表的意味。面对那钢铁幻象的折磨，宛如面对一位巨大的刺客亲临身旁。

然而，我的感官正逐渐向更为险恶的存在敞开。从浸泡着房间的硫磺暮色中，从我身旁渐渐浮现出灵魂所能目睹而不至崩解的最可怕的形体——同样是一个通体由钢铁铸就的恶魔，通体烧得白热炽红，散发着幽冥密室深处的耀目光芒。一张宛如恶意与讥讽之一切想象之钢铁化身的面孔怒视着我，其目光因极致的炽热而令人枯萎，更因其所象征的未曾设想过的内在险恶而令人毛骨悚然。我意识下方才听到的笑声出自何人之口，而顷刻间，我又再次听到了它。在他身旁，另一个宛如其孪生骨肉的魔鬼，正摇晃着一只如同周遭一切事物一样由铁条构筑的巨大摇篮，那摇篮同恶魔本身一样散发着猛烈的白炽高热。

此刻，在可能想象出的最骇人听闻的亵渎颂歌中——或者不如说，在人类思绪从未设想过的如此可怖的亵渎中——两个恶魔齐声引吭高唱，以至于我单单听闻便感到自身变得极度邪恶。我至今仍记得那首歌的含义，尽管世间尚未创造出任何能够传达它的语言；而我亦绝不敢哪怕稍加暗示其本质，唯恐自己在任何程度上延续了除沉沦者的幽冥居所之外凡间唇舌皆无法吐露的亵渎。音乐本身的每一个音符皆与那思绪相契合，恰如象征昭示着本质；而在那铿锵刺耳的喧嚣中，夹杂着永无休止摇摆的摇篮所发出的令人发狂的嘎吱声，直到我感到自己被逼入了凶暴的绝望。突然，最近处的那个恶魔抓起一把草叉（亦由白热的钢铁铸成），猛地刺入我挣扎翻滚的侧肋，将尖叫着的我狠狠摔入了那座烈火摇篮之中。

===Original===

I sought in my torture to scale the bars; they slipped from my grasp and under my feet like the smoothest icicles. Through increasing grades of agony I lay unconsumed, tossing from side to side with the rocking of the dreadful engine, and still above me pealed the chant of blasphemy, and the eyes of demoniac sarcasm smiled at me in mockery of a mother’s gaze upon her child.
“Let us sing him,” said one of the fiends to the other, “the lullaby of Hell.” The blasphemy now changed into an awful word-picturing of eternity, unveiling what it was, and dwelling with raptures of malice upon its infinitude, its sublimity of growing pain, and its privation of all fixed points which might mark it into divisions. By emblems common to all language rather than by any vocal words, did they sing this frightful apocalypse, yet the very emblems had a sound as distinct as tongue could give them. This was one, and the only one of their representatives that I can remember. Slowly they began, “Today is father of tomorrow, tomorrow hath a son that shall beget the day succeeding.” With increasing rapidity they sang in this way, day by day, the genealogy of a thousand years, and I traced on the successive generations, without a break in one link, until the rush of their procession reached a rapidity so awful as fully to typify eternity itself; and still I fled on through that burning genesis of cycles. I feel that I do not convey my meaning, but may no one else ever understand it better!
Withered like a leaf in the breath of an oven, after millions of years I felt myself tossed upon the iron floor. The fiends had departed, the cradle was gone. I stood alone, staring into immense and empty spaces. Presently I found that I was in a colossal square, as of some European city, alone at the time of evening twilight, and surrounded by houses hundreds of stories high. I was bitterly athirst. I ran to the middle of the square, and reached it after an infinity of travel. There was a fountain carved in iron, every jet inimitably sculptured in mockery of water, yet dry as the ashes of a furnace. “I shall perish with thirst,” I cried. “Yet one more trial. There must be people in all these immense houses. Doubtless they love the dying traveler, and will give him to drink. Good friends! water! water!” A horribly deafening din poured on me from the four sides of the square.

===Chinese===

在酷刑折磨中，我试图攀爬铁栏；它们却如同最光滑的冰棱般从我的指缝与脚下滑脱。在不断升级的痛苦煎熬中，我卧倒在内未被烧尽，随着那具可怕机械的晃动左右翻滚，头顶依然回荡着亵渎的赞歌，恶魔讥刺的双眸冷笑着俯视着我，戏谑地摹仿着母亲凝视襁褓婴儿的慈目。

“让我们为他唱一曲，”其中一个恶魔对另一个说，“地狱的摇篮曲。”此时，那亵渎转变为对永恒的可怖言语描摹，揭示出永恒的本质，带着恶意的狂喜详述其无边无际、其日益加剧之痛的崇高，以及其剥夺了所有可将其划分为若干片段的定点。他们并非借助任何有声言辞，而是通过所有语言共通的象征意象来吟唱这场骇人的启示，然而那些象征本身所发出的声音，却同唇舌所能吐露的一样清晰分明。这是我所能记起的唯一一个代表。他们缓缓开唱：“今日是明日之父，明日之子将孕育后日。”以越来越快的速度，他们就这样日复一日地唱着千年的谱系，而我顺着一代又一代的脉络追溯下去，没有一丝断裂的环节，直到那行列的奔涌达到了足以完全象征永恒本身的骇人速度；而我依然在那燃烧的轮回创生中狂奔逃亡。我觉得自己并未完全传达出原意，但愿世间再无任何人比我领会得更深！

在烤炉的气息中如枯叶般枯萎，在经历了数百万年之后，我感到自己被抛掷在铁地板上。恶魔们离去了，摇篮不见了。我独自伫立，凝视着广袤空旷的空间。不多时，我发觉自己置身于某座欧洲城市般的巨大广场中央，在黄昏暮色中孤身一人，四周环绕着数百层高的楼房。我口渴难耐。我向广场中央狂奔，在经历了无尽的跋涉后终于抵达。那里有一座钢铁雕成的喷泉，每一股喷涌的水流都惟妙惟肖地雕琢着水波的模样作为嘲弄，却干涸得宛如熔炉里的死灰。“我要渴死了，”我大喊道，“再试一次。这些高楼里必定住着人。他们必定怜悯垂死的行者，会给他水喝。好心人！水啊！水！”一阵震耳欲聋的可怕喧嚣从广场的四面八方朝我倾泻而下。

===Original===

Every sash of all the hundred stories of every house in that colossal quadrangle flew up as by one spring. Awakened by my call, at every window stood a terrific maniac. Sublimely in the air above me, in front, beside me, on either hand, and behind my back, a wilderness of insane faces gnashed at me, glared, gibbered, howled, laughed horribly, hissed and cursed. At the unbearable sight I myself became insane, and, leaping up and down, mimicked them all, and drank their demented spirit.
A hand seized my arm﻿—a voice called my name. The square grew lighter﻿—it changed﻿—it slowly took a familiar aspect, and gradually I became aware that my roommate was standing before me with a lighted lamp. I sank back into his arms, crying “Water! water, Robert! For the love of heaven, water!” He passed across the room to the washstand, leaving me upon the bed, where I afterward found he had replaced me on being awakened by hearing me leap frantically up and down upon the floor. In going for the water, he seemed to be traveling over a desert plain to some far-off spring, and I hailed him on his return with the pitcher and the glass as one greets his friend restored after a long journey. No glass for me! I snatched the pitcher, and drank a Niagara of refreshment with every draught. I reveled in the ecstasy of a drinker of the rivers of al-Firdaus.
Hashish always brings with it an awakening of perception which magnifies the smallest sensation till it occupies immense boundaries. The hashish-eater who drinks during his highest state of exaltation almost invariably supposes that he is swallowing interminable floods, and imagines his throat an abyss which is becoming gorged by the sea. Repeatedly, as in an agony of thirst I have clutched some small vessel of water and tipped it at my lips, I have felt such a realization of an overwhelming torrent that, with my throat still charred, I have put the water away, lest I should be drowned by the flow.
With the relighting of the lamp my terrors ceased. The room was still immense, yet the iron of its structure, in the alembic of that heavenly light, had been transmuted into silver and gold. Beamy spars, chased by some unearthly graver, supported the roof above me, and a mellow glory transfused me, shed from sunny panels that covered the walls.

===Chinese===

在那座巨大四合院落中，每栋楼房所有数百层的所有窗扇，皆如同一根弹簧触动般猛然弹起。被我的呼喊所惊醒，每一扇窗前都伫立着一个可怖的疯子。在我头顶上方的虚空中、在前方、在身旁、在左右两侧、在我的背后，一片由狂乱面孔构成的旷野向我咬牙切齿、怒目而视、胡言乱语、号啕嚎叫、发出可怕的狂笑、嘶鸣咒骂。面对这难以承受的惨象，我自己亦陷入癫狂，上蹿下跳，摹仿着他们所有人，饮尽了他们疯癫的狂灵。

一只手抓住了我的胳膊。是我的室友。“看在上帝的份上，”他大喊道，“你到底怎么了？”他的出现将我拉回了清醒。“水，”这是我唯一能吐出的字眼。他点亮了灯，去门厅取水。过了好一阵子，我才收拢思绪弄清自己身在何处。起初，我还以为他是广场上的疯子之一，惊恐地缩身躲避他；但他留在桌上燃烧的煤气灯焰，渐渐让我重新认识了现实。门厅的门敞开着，在外面走廊的地板上，我能听见他的脚步声渐行渐远，随后又折返回来。没有任何声音比寻常人踩在地板上的脚步声，更能抚慰处于恐惧之中的食大麻者了。在他去取水的过程里，他仿佛跨越了一片荒漠平原前往遥远的泉眼；当他带着水罐与水杯归来时，我迎接他的心情，宛如迎接一位历经长途跋涉平安归来的挚友。我顾不上用杯子！我一把夺过水罐，每一大口咽下的清凉都如同一座尼亚加拉瀑布（Niagara）。我沉醉在畅饮极乐仙境（al-Firdaus）之河的狂喜之中。

大麻总会带来感知的觉醒，将最微小的感觉放大至占据无边无际的界限。处于最高亢奋状态下的食大麻者在饮水时，几乎无一例外地误以为自己吞下了浩瀚连绵的洪流，想象着自己的咽喉成了一道被大海填塞吞没的深渊。不知有多少次，当我在焦渴的痛苦中抓过某个小巧的水器凑到唇边时，我都深切感受到一股吞噬一切的激流，以至于喉头虽依然干涸灼痛，我却不得不将水推开，生怕自己会被水流活活淹死。

随着灯火重明，我的恐惧平息了。房间依然宏阔，然而其结构的冰冷钢铁，在那神圣之光的蒸馏炼金器（alembic）中，已然被蜕变为白银与纯金。由超凡脱俗的雕刻师精心雕琢的光芒四射的椽木支撑着我头顶的屋顶，覆在墙上的向阳壁板洒下一片温润的荣光，渗透并滋养着我的身心。

===Original===

Out of this hall of grammarye I suddenly passed through a crystal gate, and found myself again in the world outside. Through a valley carpeted with roses I marched proudly at the head of a grand army, and the most triumphant music pealed from all my legions. In the symphony joined many an unutterable instrument, bugles and ophicleides, harps and cymbals, whose wondrous peals seemed to say, “We are self-conscious; we exult like human souls.” There were roses everywhere﻿—roses under foot, roses festooning the lattices at our sides, roses showering a prodigal flush of beauty from the arches of an arbor overhead. Down the valley I gained glimpses of dreamy lawns basking in a Claude Lorraine sunlight. Over them multitudes of rosy children came leaping to throw garlands on my victorious road, and singing paeans to me with the voices of cherubs. Nations that my sword had saved ran bounding through the flowery walls of my avenue to cry “Our hero﻿—our savior,” and prostrate themselves at my feet. I grew colossal in a delirium of pride. I felt myself the centre of all the world’s immortal glory. As once before the ecstasy of music had borne me from the body, so now I floated out of it in the intensity of my triumph. As the last cord was dissolved, I saw all the attendant splendors of my march fade away, and became once more conscious of my room restored to its natural state.
Not a single hallucination remained. Surrounding objects resumed their wonted look, yet a wonderful surprise broke in upon me. In the course of my delirium, the soul, I plainly discovered, had indeed departed from the body. I was that soul utterly divorced from the corporeal nature, disjoined, clarified, purified. From the air in which I hovered I looked down upon my former receptacle. Animal life, with all its processes, still continued to go on; the chest heaved with the regular rise and fall of breathing, the temples throbbed, and the cheek flushed. I scrutinized the body with wonderment; it seemed no more to concern me than that of another being. I do not remember, in the course of the whole experience I have had of hashish, a more singular emotion than I felt at that moment.

===Chinese===

穿过这间魔法之厅（grammarye），我骤然穿过一道水晶之门，发觉自己重新回到了外面的世界。穿过铺满玫瑰的花谷，我昂首挺胸走在浩荡大军的最前列，最辉煌的凯旋乐章从我所有的军团中鸣响齐奏。在那交响乐中，加入了许多难以名状的乐器，军号与奥菲克莱德号（ophicleides）、竖琴与钹，那神奇的齐鸣仿佛在说：“我们亦有自我意识；我们宛如人类的心灵般狂欢雀跃。”到处皆是玫瑰——脚下踩着玫瑰，身侧的格栅上缀满玫瑰花环，头顶藤架拱廊倾洒下一阵阵奢华绚丽的玫瑰芳华。顺谷望去，我瞥见如梦如幻的草坪沐浴在克劳德·洛兰式的日光之中。成群结队面色红润的孩童欢跃跳窜着穿过草坪，将花环抛洒在我胜利的大道上，用小天使（cherubs）般的嗓音向我吟唱赞歌。我的利刃拯救的万国国民跃过我大道两侧的花墙，高呼着“我们的英雄——我们的救主”，拜倒在我的脚下。在骄傲的谵妄中，我变得高大如泰坦。我感到自己成了全世界不朽荣光的中心。正如先前音乐的狂喜曾将我带离肉体一样，此刻在胜利的极度亢奋中，我从肉身中飘逸而出。当最后一根纽带解开时，我目睹行军伴随的一切辉煌尽数隐退，再次意识到自己的房间恢复了自然原貌。

没有留下一丝幻觉。周围的物体恢复了往日的模样，然而一阵奇妙的惊愕骤然降临在我心头。在谵妄的历程中，我清晰地发现，灵魂确实已经脱离了肉体。我便是那个与肉体本性彻底决裂、脱离、澄澈并净化的灵魂。从我盘旋的虚空中，我低头俯视着我先前的容器。肉体的生命机能，连同其所有的运作过程，依然在继续运行；胸膛随着规律的呼吸起伏而隆起凹陷，太阳穴在跳动，双颊泛着红晕。我带着惊诧打量着这具身躯；它似乎同另一个生灵的身躯一样，再也与我毫无干系。在所有的大麻体验历程中，我不记得还有哪一种情感比我那一刻所感受到的更为奇特。

===Original===

The spirit discerned itself as possessed of all the human capacities, intellect, susceptibility, and will﻿—saw itself complete in every respect; yet, like a grand motor, it had abandoned the machine which it once energized, and in perfect independence stood apart. In the prerogative of my spiritual nature I was restrained by no objects of a denser class. To myself I was visible and tangible, yet I knew that no material eyes could see me. Through the walls of the room I was able to pass and repass, and through the ceiling to behold the stars unobscured. This was neither hallucination nor dream. The sight of my reason was preternaturally intense, and I remembered that this was one of the states which frequently occur to men immediately before their death has become apparent to lookers-on, and also in the more remarkable conditions of trance. That such a state is possible is incontestably proved by many cases on record in which it has fallen under the observation of students most eminent in physico-psychical science.
A voice of command called on me to return into the body, saying in the midst of my exultation over what I thought was my final disenfranchisement from the corporeal, “The time is not yet.” I returned, and again felt the animal nature joined to me by its mysterious threads of conduction. Once more soul and body were one.

===Chinese===

心灵认清自身具备人类的一切才能、理智、感知力与意志——看到自己在各方面皆完备无缺；然而，正如一台伟大的原动机，它已经遗弃了它曾赋予活力的机器，在完全的独立中超然卓立。凭借我属灵本质的特权，我不受任何更致密物质客体的阻碍。对我自身而言，我是可见且可触的，但我知晓没有任何肉眼凡胎能够看见我。我能够自如穿透房间的墙壁往返自如，能够穿透天花板凝望毫无遮拦的璀璨繁星。这既非幻觉，亦非梦境。我理性的视界异乎寻常地敏锐，我忆起这正是行将就木者在旁观者察觉其临终之前频频出现的状态之一，亦存在于那些更非同寻常的昏迷出神境况之中。此类状态的存在可能，已被文献中记载的诸多案例无可争辩地加以证实，在这些案例中，该现象曾受到心身物理科学领域最卓越的研究者的严谨观察。

一声号令呼唤我重返肉身，正当我为自己自以为最终脱离了肉体束缚而欢欣鼓舞时，那个声音说道：“时辰尚未到。”我归来了，再次感受到肉体生命机能通过其神秘的传导纽带与我重新相连。灵魂与肉体再次合二为一。

## VI The Mysteries of the Life-Sign Gemini / 第六章 双子生命之象的奥秘
===Original===

In this vision the conception of our human duality was presented to me in a manner more striking than ever before. Hitherto it had been more a suggestion than a proof; now it appeared in the light of an intuition. A wonderful field of questions is opened by such an experience, and I am constrained to sketch a few of them as they have occurred to myself.
1st: Are the animal and spiritual conjoint parts of the same life, or two different lives which intensely interact, yet are not altogether dependent upon each other for their continuance?
That the soul is dependent upon aught that we call material for the preservation of its highest functions, very few men will feel disposed to assert. Yet we are all exceedingly loth to concede that the animal has a distinct life of its own, which, for some time after the dissolution of the ties which bind it to the spiritual, might continue to throb on unimpaired. Your critic, who aims altogether at uses comprehended in bread, meat, and broadcloth, may ask, “If it be so, of what practical utility would it be to discover it?” A sufficient answer lies in the fact that men would know one more truth. A truth tested and established may lie for centuries, mildewed and rusted, in the armory of knowledge, until some great soul comes along, draws it out of the rubbish, buckles it on, rushes into the conflict, and with it pries open the portals of one more promised land of blessing for the human race. Gunpowder is a truth; wise men sneer at the monk’s obstreperous plaything. The years float calmly on; that plaything strikes the cliffs of Dover, and as they go toppling down to leave a highway for the nations, contemned truth vindicates her uses with a triumphant voice of thunder.
But there is also a tangible utility in this discovery of an independent animal life (supposing it to be made) which arises out of the fact that we should thus possess much higher notions of the spiritual than we have at present. In the desire to make the body entirely dependent on the soul for all its processes, we have linked the two in so close a marriage that the soul itself has become materialized by contact in our conceptions.

===Chinese===

在这幅幻象中，关于人类双重性的概念，以一种比以往任何时候都更加震撼的方式展现在我眼前。在此之前，它更多只是一种暗示而非证据；此刻，它却借由直觉的光芒显现出来。这样一番体验开启了一片奇妙的探究领域，我情不自禁地勾勒出自己在心头涌现的几个疑问。

其一：肉体生命与属灵生命，究竟是同一段生命相契相连的组成部分，还是两段激烈互动、但在各自的维系上并不全然彼此依存的独立生命？

要说灵魂维系其最高机能必须依赖于我们称之为物质的任何事物，恐怕极少有人愿意做出这般断言。然而，我们大家却都极其不情愿承认：肉体本身拥有其独特的独立生命，在断绝了将其与精神相系纽带之后的一段时间里，它仍可能完好无损地持续悸动。那些眼里只有面包、肉食与粗呢等实际功用的批评家或许会问：“即便如此，发现这一点又有何实际用处呢？”一个充分的回答就在于：人类将多认识一桩真理。一桩经过检验并得以确立的真理，可能在知识的军械库中生霉锈蚀数个世纪，直到某位伟大的灵魂降临，将其从瓦砾堆中抽拔而出，披挂在身，冲入阵前，并借此为人类撬开又一片赐福的应许之地的大门。火药便是一桩真理；聪明人曾对修道士那喧闹的玩具报以嗤笑。岁月从容流逝；那玩具轰击着多佛尔（Dover）的悬崖，当悬崖轰然坍塌为列国让出通途时，备受蔑视的真理便用雷霆般胜利的声音为自己的功用辩护。

然而，发现独立的肉体生命（假定该发现成立）亦有其切实的实用价值，因为我们将因此对精神本身获得远比当下高尚得多的观念。出于让肉体在一切机能运作上完全依赖灵魂的愿望，我们把两者捆绑在如此紧密的联姻之中，以至于在我们的观念里，灵魂本身因与之接触而沦为了物质化的存在。

===Original===

What we call spirit is, after all, when its vague and variable boundaries are somewhat accurately drawn, nothing but an exceedingly rarefied mist, capable, to be sure, of self-conscious phenomena, but nevertheless subject to most of the conditions of matter. We grant, indeed, that after death the interior eyes may see without the mediation of our present lens and retina, but scout the idea that those eyes, in this world, ever employ a power which, after a few years, they shall keep in constant activity forever. Now, if we can more definitely mark the line between the spiritual and the animal as between two independent lives hinged on each other, yet not interpenetrating, we shall have done much to glorify the soul and reinstate it in its proper reverence.
Not to assert the separate of the animal life as proven, let us look at some singular phenomena which, by such an hypothesis, would be explained, and (as it seems to me) by such a one only.
1st: In surgical operations performed while the patient lay under the influence of an anesthetic, as chloroform or ether, I have witnessed contortions of the whole muscular system, and heard outcries so fearful that it was impossible to persuade the lookers-on that the application of the instrument was not causing the severest agony. Upon one occasion I myself stood by a man who was to suffer a difficult dental operation, and with my own hands administered chloroform to him. All the usual symptoms of complete anesthesia ensuing, I signaled the dentist to begin his work. The moment that the instrument came into successful operation, the patient uttered a harrowing cry of pain and struggled convulsively, at the same time entreating the operator to stop. I was persuaded, from former cases of a similar nature, that the man had no consciousness of pain, and so advised the dentist. From motives of humanity, however, the latter desisted when his work was but partly accomplished, and, having extracted a single tooth instead of the several which were to be drawn, permitted the seeming sufferer to return to his natural state. He presently awoke, as from a dream; and on being asked whether he endured great torture, he laughed at the idea, denying that he had even been aware of the application of the forceps, although fully self-conscious internally during the whole effect of the anesthetic.

===Chinese===

所谓精神，归根结底，当其模糊多变的边界被稍加精确地勾勒出来时，不过是一种极其稀薄的雾气；诚然它具备自我意识现象的能力，却依然受制于物质的大多数条件。我们确实承认，死后内在的双眼无须借助当下的晶状体与视网膜便能视物，却对内在之眼在此世间便能施展那种在数年之后将永恒运作的力量嗤之以鼻。如今，如果我们能更明确地划清精神与肉体之间的界线——将二者视作彼此相系却互不渗透的两段独立生命——我们便极大地荣耀了灵魂，并恢复了它应有的崇高尊严。

即便不把肉体生命的独立性视作已成定论，且让我们来看看某些奇异的现象；依循这一假说，这些现象将迎刃而解，而且（在我看来）唯有依循这一假说方能索解。

其一：在患者处于氯仿或乙醚等麻醉剂影响下施行的外科手术中，我曾目睹过整套肌肉系统的抽搐痉挛，听闻过如此凄厉的惨叫，以至于根本无法让旁观者相信器械的施用并未造成极度的痛苦。有一次，我亲自守在一名即将接受复杂牙科手术的男子身旁，亲手为他施用氯仿。待完全麻醉的一切寻常体征出现后，我示意牙医开始操作。器械刚一落手施术的刹那，患者便发出一声令人毛骨悚然的剧痛惨叫并剧烈抽搐挣扎，同时苦苦哀求施术者住手。根据以往类似性质的案例，我坚信该男子并无痛觉意识，并据此建议牙医继续。然而出于人道考量，牙医在手术仅完成一部分时便停了手，只拔除了一颗牙齿而非原定要拔的数颗，便任由这位看似备受煎熬的受术者恢复自然状态。他不久便醒来，宛如做了一场梦；当被问及是否承受了巨大痛苦时，他对此一笑置之，断然否认自己曾察觉到拔牙钳的触碰，尽管在麻醉药效的全程中他在内在层面完全保持着自我意识。

===Original===

I believe I am only stating one of many cases which fall under the almost daily observation of men of wide experience in the surgical profession. Although far from being an expert myself, I have been an eyewitness to two such instances.
Now what is it, or who is it that is suffering tortures so great that the face, the lips, the limbs must give vent to them in such intensity of expression? The soul has been all the time lying in a delicious calm of meditation, or gliding through a succession of strange images, whose order was not once broken by the thrill of pain. It frequently remembers its visions, and can repeat them coherently; it would certainly have recollected, if it had ever known them, some traits of an experience so utterly discordant as suffering.
An inference directly suggests itself. Where all the outer phenomena of torture have been witnessed, the anesthetic has not so much affected the body as the ties which unite it with the soul. A temporary disjunction has taken place between the two, and the animal nature has been suffering while the spiritual, completely insulated, was left to its own free activity.
2nd: I believe it is gradually becoming conceded that the agonies which universal belief once attached to the idea of death are rather imaginary than real. Yet the hour of dissolution is almost invariably accompanied by groans and contortions, which tell tales of the bitter pang felt somewhere in the depths of that mysterious being which is becoming disjoined. While the dying man, if still fully conscious, frequently asserts that he is in ecstasy beyond compare, tense muscles and writhing limbs are telling another story. What is it that is suffering?
3rd: There have been instances of the trance state which throw an additional light upon this question, or involve it in deeper mystery, according to the mental temperament of the man who considers them. It is needless to quote the case of Tennant in our own country, and many cataleptic and hypnotic states which have fallen under private notice, when an argument *a fortiori* may be drawn from the remarkable phenomena which but a few years ago transpired under the eyewitness of many eminent men of the medical and other professions in India.1 So important a field of inquiry did these phenomena seem to open, that Dr.

===Chinese===

我相信自己所陈述的，不过是外科界经验卓著的人士几乎每日都能观察到的诸多案例之一而已。尽管我自己绝非行家里手，却也亲眼目睹过两次此类情形。

那么，究竟是什么，或者究竟是谁，在遭受着如此剧烈的酷刑折磨，以至于面容、双唇与四肢必须以如此强烈的表达来加以宣泄？灵魂自始至终皆静卧在沉思的甜美安谧之中，或滑翔穿过一连串奇妙的意象，其秩序未曾一度被痛苦的震颤所打乱。它常常能忆起自己的幻象，并能条理清晰地复述它们；倘若它曾体会过，它必然会忆起某种同痛苦一样格格不入的体验痕迹。

一个推论油然而生：在目睹了酷刑的所有外部现象之处，麻醉剂与其说是作用于肉体，倒不如说是作用于将肉体与灵魂维系在一起的纽带。两者之间发生了暂时的分离，当肉体机能遭受痛苦时，完全处于绝缘状态的精神却得以任由其自由活动。

其二：我相信人们正逐渐公认，世人曾经普遍归咎于死亡概念的剧烈痛苦，与其说是真实的，不如说是想象出来的。然而，肉体消亡的时刻几乎无一例外地伴随着呻吟与抽搐，诉说着在那正处于分离过程中的神秘生命深处的某处，所体会到的刻骨剧痛。当垂死之人若尚保持完全清醒时，常常断言自己正沉浸在无可比拟的狂喜之中，紧绷的肌肉与翻滚挣扎的四肢却在讲述着另一番光景。究竟是什么在承受痛苦？

其三：曾有出神迷离状态（trance）的案例，或为这一问题提供了新的线索，或将其卷入更深层的神秘之中，端看审视者持何种思维秉性。无需援引我国腾南特（Tennant）的案例，以及私下留意到的诸多强直性昏厥与催眠状态；仅从数年前在印度许多医学界及其他行业的显赫人士亲眼目睹下所发生的惊人现象中，便能得出更为强有力的论据（*a fortiori*）。这一现象似乎开启了一片如此重大的探究领域，以至于

===Original===

Braid, of Edinburgh, a physician of considerable fame, made it the groundwork of a book, condensed, yet valuable for its research, upon the trance condition, and the scientific mind throughout Great Britain took a lively interest in the subject. A fakir presented himself at one of the Company’s stations, and proffered the singular request that he might be buried alive. Though not much astonished at any possible petition coming from one of an order of men so wildly fanatic as those who infest India with their monstrous devotions and insatiable alms-begging, the servants of the Company still treated him as insane, and answered his request with corresponding neglect. Still, the fakir insisted upon their compliance, asserting that he possessed the power of separating soul and body at will, and was able to live without air or food for the space of thirty days. Upon his producing native witnesses who fully corroborated his statement, he obtained a more deferential attention to his demand. As his reason for asking sepulture, he stated the desire for a more complete abstraction of soul than he could attain above ground and among the things of sense, positively assuring his questioners that this abstraction, as he had tested by repeated experiments, was in no danger of proving fatal to the body.
At last, then, his petition was granted. By an effort of will he threw himself into the ecstatic or trance state, and when the vital processes had become absolutely imperceptible, and he lay to all appearance dead, he was closely wrapped in a winding-sheet, and, for fear of imposition, buried in a tightly-masoned tomb. The opening was then filled with earth, and the mound thus raised above him thickly sown with barley. A Mohammedan guard (the last in the world which would be likely to connive at the cheat of a disciple of Brahm) was stationed about the grave night and day.
