The barley grew up undisturbed till the month was accomplished, and, at the expiration of that time, hundreds of people thronged to be present at the disentombing of the fakir. Among them were grave men, men of calm and scientific minds, and many utterly incredulous of the possibility that human life could have been sustained from inner sources through so long a period. Every test was thus present which could make evidence of any fact conclusive beyond doubt.

===Chinese===

爱丁堡负有盛名的医生布雷德博士（Dr. Braid），以此为基础撰写了一本探讨出神状态的著作，内容凝练却极富研究价值，全英格兰的科学界亦对该课题产生了浓厚兴趣。一名印度苦行僧（fakir）来到东印度公司的某个驻地，提出了一个奇特的要求：希望被活埋。尽管面对来自如此狂热群体——他们以离奇的苦修祈祷和无厌的乞讨充斥印度——的任何匪夷所思的诉求本不足为奇，公司的差役起初仍将他视作疯子，对他的要求不予理睬。然而苦行僧坚决要求他们照办，声称自己拥有随意分离灵魂与肉体的力量，能够在无空气、无食物的状态下存活三十天。在他召来完全证实其说法的当地证人后，他的要求得到了更为审慎尊重的对待。至于请求入土安葬的理由，他陈述说，这是为了追求一种比在地面上和感官俗物中所能达到的更彻底的灵魂超脱，并向质询者信誓旦旦地保证：经过反复测试，这种超脱绝不会对肉体造成致命危害。

最终，他的请求得到了应允。凭借意志力，他令自己进入了出神入定的恍惚状态；当生命体征完全不可察觉、外表看起来已然气绝身亡时，他被裹尸布紧紧包裹起来；为防作弊欺诈，他被安葬在一座严丝合缝的砖石墓穴中。墓穴入口随后用泥土填平，堆起的坟丘上密密麻麻地播撒了大麦。一支穆斯林卫队（世上最不可能纵容梵天信徒欺诈作弊的人）日夜守卫在坟墓四周。大麦在无人惊扰下破土生长，直至一个月期满；时限一到，数百人涌至现场，争相目睹苦行僧的出土。在他们之中，有严肃之士，有冷静睿智的科学头脑，亦有许多人根本不信人类生命能够凭借内在源泉维系如此漫长的时日。一切足以证明事实确凿无疑的检验条件皆已齐备。

===Original===

The body of the fakir was found unaltered by decay, yet shriveled to a mummy. Means of restoration were used very similar to those employed in bringing a cataleptic patient to consciousness. Presently the seemingly dead man began to breathe, his color returned, and before the close of the day, as the nutriment which was given him was assimilated, all his functions were in their ordinary activity.
A more complete separation of the animal and spiritual probably never existed without death, yet the two lives, through the whole period of sepulture, were sustained apart without the slightest consciousness in the soul that the body was growing emaciated, convulsed, and juiceless. Many of the eyewitnesses to this wonderful experiment are living to the present day.
Upon the theory of these independent existences it may be asked, “How is death possible at all to the animal?” We reply, In most cases, doubtless, the animal dies first, and the spiritual deserts it afterward; but, wherever the spiritual is the more powerfully agonized of the two, in the very shock of its exertions to depart it may bear the animal away with it, which, not being immortal, has no possible residence outside of the body, but instantly perishes. Yet when, as by the gentle disentanglement of patient fingers, the ligaments of the corporeal life are unwound from about the soul, the latter, undestroyed, may still remain through its allotted day of endurance. If this be more than mere visionary conjecture, it accounts for the unchanged appearance of bodies disentombed after a hundred years, and the relics unconsumed by time, which, in the world’s reaction from hyper-credulity, we have so long been apt to classify with the other legends of the *Vita Sanctorum*.
2. Another question suggested by the experience of my own duality is this: If the two existences are independent, may not the fact account for that blind feeling which almost every man has experienced, that he has lived previously to his present form in other and entirely different states? The idea of the metempsychosis was never, indeed, made the central one of any system of philosophy until the time of Pythagoras.

===Chinese===

苦行僧的躯体被发现未受腐败侵蚀，只是干瘪得宛如一具木乃伊。人们采用了与唤醒强直性昏厥患者非常相似的复苏手段。很快，看似死去的男子开始呼吸，面色转润，在当天结束之前，随着喂服的养分被吸收同化，他所有的机能皆恢复了寻常的活力。

在未曾真正死亡的情况下，肉体与精神大概从未经历过比这更彻底的分离；然而在整个埋葬期间，两段生命彼此独立维系，灵魂丝毫未曾意识到肉体正在日益消瘦、抽搐与脱水。这一奇妙实验的诸多目击者，至今仍健在人世。

基于这两段独立存在的理论，或许有人会问：“那么肉体究竟如何才可能死亡呢？”我们回答：毫无疑问，在大多数情况下，是肉体先死，随后精神才弃它而去；但是，只要两者之中精神承受了更强烈的痛苦煎熬，在它挣扎离去的震颤之中，它就可能将肉体一并带走，而肉体并非永生之物，在躯体之外毫无容身之所，故而立时毙命。然而，当宛如被耐心的手指轻柔解开一样，肉体生命的韧带从灵魂周围缓缓松脱时，未受毁损的肉体仍能在其被赋予的承受之日内安然存续。倘若这不仅是凭空幻想的推测，它便能合理解释百年后出土的遗体为何容貌未改，以及未被岁月销蚀的圣骨遗骸——在世人从盲目轻信走向反弹的过程中，我们长久以来一直习惯将它们归为《圣徒传》（*Vita Sanctorum*）中的荒诞传奇。

我自身双重性体验所启示的另一个问题是：如果这两段存在是独立的，这一事实是否能解释几乎每个人都曾体验过的那种盲目模糊的直觉——即他在当前的形体之前，曾以其他截然不同的状态生存过？灵魂转世（metempsychosis）的概念，直到毕达哥拉斯时代才真正成为某种哲学体系的核心。

===Original===

He was the first of whom we have historic mention to scale off from the original gem the laminae of grosser Egyptian and Indian fable, which covered it like a later deposit (and he had reasons for doing so, which we think will be proved, to a strong probability at least, in a future portion of this narrative); yet, after all, metempsychosis, as a fact, has been dimly felt by universal humanity, and even at the present day presents itself at times so strongly to many a mind as almost to carry the conviction of an intuition.
But, upon our hypothesis, can the idea be accounted for? Let us see.
Except in the prerogative of the peculiar quality of that life which animates it, the body has no more claims to reverence than the same number of pounds of alkali, water, iron, and other chemicals composing it, in any other form.
But for the energizing, vital element of the particular rank in the scale of vitality which energizes man, he would be worthy of quite as much consideration were he sealed up in carboys, poured into pitchers, blown into bladders, and tied up in brown paper parcels. His body has not the faintest stamp of originality. As bovine muscle he existed long ago in the food which nourished his parents; still farther back he was eaten by an ox in the form of some succulent weed of the pasture, and that very weed educed him from the soil through microscopic tubes by capillary attraction. Wash this soil, and he will be deposited in the form of a precipitate; yet after all this investigation of his material genealogy, we have only arrived at the same result which could be attained by any skillful chemist who would undergo the labor of taking him to pieces in his present state, and subject him to adequate tests. The man of visionary mind may sit down before one solitary cabbage, and find food for his thought, if not for his palate, in the reflection, “Truly thou mightest have been my brother.”
Now, without the least shadow of a wish to prove matter self-conscious, may we not hold it possible that the particles entering into our corporeal composition still preserve some subtle properties (not memory, be it understood) of the other bodies through which they have passed, which, being felt by our own animal nature, are suggested by it to the spiritual as a ground for the idea of metempsychosis?

===Chinese===

在有史可查的记录中，他是第一位从最初的原石上剥去粗鄙的埃及与印度神话层积物的人（他有理由这样做，而我们认为在本书后文中，这些理由至少将以极大的概率得以证实）；然而归根结底，转世作为一种事实，已被普世人类隐约感知，甚至在今天，它有时仍如此强烈地浮现于许多人的心头，以至于几乎带有一种直觉的笃信。

但是，依循我们的假说，这一观念能够得到解释吗？且让我们来看。

除却赋予其活力的那种生命独特质性的特权之外，肉体所应享有的尊崇，绝不比以任何其他形态构筑它的同等重量的碱、水、铁及其他化学物质更多分毫。

若非在生命谱系中特定阶位的赋予活力的元精赋予人类以生机，人类被封入坛罐、注入水壶、吹入气囊、用牛皮纸包裹扎紧时，所享有的器重也理当与活着时完全一样。他的肉体没有哪怕一丝一毫独创性的印记。早先，它曾作为牛肉的肌纤维存在于滋养他父母的食物之中；再往更早追溯，它曾作为牧场上多汁的野草被一头公牛吞下，而那株野草正是通过毛细管引力将它从土壤微管中萃取而出的。冲洗这片泥土，他便会以沉淀物的形式沉积下来；然而，在对他物质谱系进行了这番彻底的追溯之后，我们所得到的结论，也不过与任何一位肯费心在当下状态下将他拆解并施以充分化验测试的熟练化学家所能得出的结论如出一辙。富于幻想心智之人尽可端坐在一颗孤独的卷心菜前，在“你确实可能曾是我的兄弟”的沉思中，为他的思绪——即使不是为了他的味蕾——寻得精神食粮。

如今，在毫无意图证明物质拥有自我意识的情况下，我们难道不能认为存在这样一种可能：进入我们肉体构成的微粒，依然保留着它们曾流经的其他躯体的某些微妙特质（须明白，这并非记忆）；这些特质被我们自身的肉体本性所感知，并由其向属灵生命提示，从而构成了转世观念的根据？

===Original===

The body will then be that part of us which has really transmigrated, while the soul is original.
The idea that the soul has ever transmigrated leads us into painful, disgusting, irrational, and irreligious conclusions. But grant that, in the animal life, a blind perception exists of peculiar qualities in the corporeal particles, arising out of former conditions through which they have passed, and we can then see how it may be possible for the spiritual to sympathize with the animal to such a degree as to etherealize these perceptions into a dreamy echo of its own former being. The problem, therefore, stands thus: Both for the sake of right and reason we must utterly disown the idea of spiritual metempsychosis. How, then, can we explain the fact of its universality among the race? We offer our hypothesis.

===Chinese===

如此一来，肉体便成了我们自身真正经历过轮回流转的那一部分，而灵魂则是独创原始的。

认为灵魂曾经历轮回流转的观念，会将我们引向痛苦、令人作呕、荒谬悖理且背弃宗教的结论。然而，假定在肉体生命中，存在着一种对肉体微粒源自过往所经境况之独特特质的盲目感知，我们便能明了：属灵生命何以能与肉体产生如此程度的共鸣，以至于将这些感知升华为自身前生如梦如幻的回响。因此，问题便摆在眼前：无论为了正道还是理性，我们都必须彻底摒弃灵魂转世的观念。那么，我们又该如何解释该观念在人类中的普遍存在呢？我们谨提出我们的假说。

## VII The Night of Apotheosis / 第七章 封神之夜
===Original===

It may be thought strange that, after that experience of infinite agony which I have last related, I should ever take hashish again. “Surely,” it will be said, “another experiment with the drug would be a daring venture into the realms of insanity and death. The gentlest name that could be applied to it is foolhardiness.”
The morning immediately succeeding my night of horror found me as vigorous and buoyant as I ever was in my life. No pain, no feeling of lassitude remained, and on my face there was not the faintest record of the tortures through which I had passed. In the midst of the very astonishment with which I noted this fact, I felt assured that I had done myself no injury. Yet, mentally, I had the conception of being older by many years than on the night previous; all past experiences in life seemed separated from me by a measureless gulf of duration, and when the demon faces or hellish songs of my vision flashed up into memory, I shuddered and turned my head as if they were close at hand. Quietly I made a resolve that I would experiment with the drug of sorcery no more, for I dreaded another plunge into the abyss of terror as I dreaded hell itself.
Slowly passed away from my mind the image of my sufferings. The elastic force of thought threw off the weight of all direful remembrances, and whenever I recalled my last night of vision it was only to dwell with tenderness upon the roses of my valley, and exult in the echo of the paeans which had glorified my march. So beautiful did such memories make the inner world, that I wearied of the outer till it became utterly distasteful, like a heavy tragedy seen for the fortieth time. I tried in vain to detect in the landscape that ever-welling freshness of life which hashish unveils; trees were meaningless wood, the clouds a vapory sham. I thirsted for insight, adventure, strange surprises, and mystical discoveries. I took hashish again.
I was sitting at the tea-table when the thrill smote me. I had handed my cup to Miss M’Ilvaine to be replenished for the first time, and she was about restoring it to me brimming with that draught “which cheers but not inebriates.” I should be loth to calculate the arc through which her hand appeared to me to travel on its way to the side of my plate.

===Chinese===

在我讲述了上次经历的那番无尽痛苦之后，我竟然还会再次吸食大麻，这或许会让人觉得不可思议。“想必，”有人会说，“对这种药物的另一次实验，无异于鲁莽闯入癫狂与死亡的领地。对此所能给予的最温和的称谓，也是有勇无谋的蛮干。”

然而，就在那惊魂之夜过后的次日清晨，我发觉自己同平生任何时刻一样精神矍铄、生机盎然。没有留下一丝肉体的痛苦或倦怠感，脸上亦未曾留下我所历经折磨的最微弱痕迹。就在我注意到这一事实的极大惊异之中，我确信自己并未受到任何损伤。然而在精神层面上，我却觉得自己比前一夜苍老了许多岁；过往人生中的一切经历，似乎都与我相隔着一道深不可测的岁月鸿沟；每当幻象中恶魔的面孔或地狱的颂歌在记忆中闪现，我都会不寒而栗地转过头去，仿佛它们近在咫尺。我暗下决心，绝不再用这种巫术之药做任何实验，因为我惧怕再次跌入恐惧的深渊，恰如惧怕地狱本身。

渐渐地，受苦的意象从我心头淡去。思维的韧性卸下了所有可怖回忆的重负；每当我想起上一次幻象之夜，不过是深情地流连于幽谷中的玫瑰，沉醉于为我的行军增光的凯歌回响之中。这些回忆将内在世界装点得如此美丽，以至于我对外部世界感到厌倦，直至它变得索然无味，宛如第四十次观看一部沉闷乏味的悲剧。我徒劳地试图在寻常风景中寻觅大麻所揭示的那种源源不绝的生命生机；树木成了毫无意义的木头，云彩成了水汽构成的伪饰。我渴求洞见、冒险、奇遇以及神秘的发现。我再次服下了大麻。

当那阵战栗击中我时，我正端坐在茶桌旁。我刚把茶杯递给麦基尔韦恩小姐（Miss M’Ilvaine）第一次续杯，她正准备将斟满了“令人愉悦却不致沉醉”的琼浆茶杯递还于我，放在我的盘旁。我实在不愿去测算她的手移向我的盘边时在我眼前所划过的那道漫长弧线。

===Original===

The wall grew populous with dancing satyrs; Chinese mandarins nodded idiotically in all the corners, and I felt strongly the necessity of leaving the table before I betrayed myself.
I rose and hurried from the room. A friend of mine, thinking that I had been taken suddenly ill, immediately followed me. The look of wild delight with which I greeted him would have revealed my secret, even had I not spontaneously imparted it to him.
In the first stages of his singular life, the hashish-eater finds so much that is strange, beautiful, or appalling, that he can restrain neither his outbursts of enthusiasm nor of pain. He is big with infinite arcana, which he feels he must disclose or perish. Gradually self-control becomes with him more of a possibility, and finally it is stereotyped into a habit. In my earlier experience I found it beyond my power, even with the most agonizing efforts, to keep back the wonders which I saw, and accordingly, the moment that I found my brain expanding into the hashish-dome, I made it my wont to rush from the presence of all who ought not to share my secret. When many days had taught me lessons of self-retention, I sat frequently for hours charred in demoniac flames, or lifted into the seventh heaven of ecstasy, with a throng around me who could not have gained the faintest intimation from my manner of the processes which were going on within.
When Sam joined me I was on the eve of another journey through vast territories. I say “Sam,” for I shall take the liberty of calling all my friends by those familiar names which embody to me all that is loving, genial, and belonging to idiosyncrasy in my remembrance of them. Doubtless such a practice is discordant with courtly style in the most eminent degree. It would be much more polite to say Villiers where I meant Joe, and Cholmondeley instead of Harry; for in this way I should much more readily and thoroughly conciliate those minds which, enervated by the spicy feasts of high-life literature, are unable to find the least sapidity in the vocabulary of daily affections.

===Chinese===

墙壁上挤满了翩翩起舞的萨堤尔（satyrs）；中国官员们在各个角落里痴傻地颔首示意，我强烈感到必须在失态暴露之前离开茶桌。

我起身匆匆步出房间。我的一位朋友以为我突发急病，立即跟随其后。即便我未曾主动向他吐露，我迎接他时脸上那狂喜的神情也早已泄露了我的秘密。

在这种离奇生涯的最初阶段，食大麻者会发现如此之多奇异、壮美或骇人的事物，以至于他既无法克制热情的迸发，亦无法压抑痛苦的宣泄。他腹中饱怀着无尽的奥秘，感到若不吐露便唯有毁灭。渐渐地，自我克制对他而言日益成为可能，并最终固化为一种习惯。在我早期的经历中，我发觉即便付出最痛苦的努力，也无力隐瞒自己所目睹的奇景；因此，一旦察觉自己的大脑正在膨胀为大麻穹顶，我便习惯性地从那些不该分享我秘密的人群面前夺门而逃。当漫长的时日教会了我自持的教训之后，我常常能在魔火的炙烤中枯坐数小时，或被提升至第七重天的狂喜极境，而身旁簇拥的人群却无法从我的举止中捕捉到我内心正在经历的一切的最微弱端倪。

当萨姆（Sam）赶上我时，我正处于踏上穿行辽阔疆域的另一场漫游的前夕。我称他为“萨姆”，因为我冒昧地用那些亲切的昵称来称呼我所有的朋友，这些名字在我的回忆中凝聚了关爱、温情以及专属于他们个性的特质。毫无疑问，这种做法在极高程度上与优雅的宫廷文风大相径庭。若将乔（Joe）称为维利尔斯（Villiers），将哈里（Harry）唤作乔蒙德利（Cholmondeley），未免要客气得多；因为借由这种方式，我便能更容易、更彻底地迎合那些被上流文学的辛辣盛宴消磨得萎靡不振、在日常真挚情感的词汇中品味不出哪怕一丝滋味的读者。

===Original===

Southey, discoursing of the Doctor, has made that mirror of true-heartedness, as well as true courtesy, remark (I quote from memory), that among the most painful, though quiet and unnoticed losses which a man sustains in his passage from the infant to the graybeard, is the gradual divestment of his right to be called by the name which he heard in the nursery and on the playground. “Now,” saith Daniel Dove, with a gentle sigh, “even my wife speaks of me as ‘the Doctor.’ ” Most genial men have felt the same thing with sorrow as the “toga virilis” slowly wrapped them closer and closer into the reserve of middle life, hiding those earlier insignia of frankness and good-fellowship which no longer give them a claim to be hailed with affectionate intimacy, yet which every true man will still bear with lively remembrance upon his heart of hearts.
I have always entertained a deep grudge against the cold and courtly Cicero for that unworthy sneer launched at the friendship between Catiline and Tongilius, “*Quem amare in praetexta coeperat.*” It was in the style of Cicero, indeed, yet not in the style of the truly noble man, nor of one who holds in fitting reverence the bond of our earlier humanities. It seems impossible to conceive how anyone dignified with the better and deeper feelings of our nature should become aware, with any other sentiment than pain, that he is surviving the days when a more intimate confidence and unworldly simplicity gave genial friends a right to address him and treat him as a brother.
I shall therefore, without any apology, unless this digression may be styled so, call all the nearer and dearer companions of my youth by those names which sound as the sweetest echoes of the Past in the chambers of my memory, since the strings with which they vibrate in unison can not too long be kept thrilling in any heart that would not neglect all music beyond that with which the march of our dusty life in the exterior keeps step.
I have said that when Sam joined me I was once more filled with the frenzy of travel. I besought him to go with me, painting in the most glowing tints the treasures which such a gigantic tour as I had laid out would add to his acquaintance with the grand Kosmos. He consented to become my compagnon de voyage for a few hundred miles, at any rate, and directly we set out.

===Chinese===

索斯（Southey）在谈及《医生》（The Doctor）时，曾借这位真诚与真正谦恭典范之口评论道（我凭记忆引述）：在一个人由襁褓婴孩走向白发老叟的历程中所承受的最痛苦、却又最沉寂且不为人知的失去之一，便是逐渐被剥夺了被唤作他在育儿室与操场上所听到的名字的权利。“如今，”丹尼尔·多夫（Daniel Dove）轻声叹息道，“连我的妻子都称呼我为‘医生’了。”随着象征成年的“成年袍（toga virilis）”缓缓将他们越来越紧地包裹进中年的矜持之中，遮蔽了昔日坦率与深厚情谊的徽记，大多数热忱的人都曾带着哀伤体会过同样的失落；尽管这些徽记不再赋予他们被亲昵相称的权利，但每一个真性情的男子汉依然会在内心最深处对它们保留着鲜活的记忆。

对于冷酷文雅的西塞罗（Cicero）针对喀提林（Catiline）与通吉利乌斯（Tongilius）之间的友谊所发出的那声刻薄嘲弄——“*早在身穿童袍时便相爱（Quem amare in praetexta coeperat）*”——我始终深怀愤懑。这确实符合西塞罗的行文风格，却绝非一位真正高尚之士的胸襟，亦非一位对我们早年人性羁绊怀有应有敬意之人的做派。实在无法想象，任何拥有我们天性中更美好、更深沉情感的高尚之人，在意识到自己已经苟活过了那个因更亲密的信任与不谙世故的纯真而让挚友有权称呼他、待他如手足的岁月时，心中除了痛苦还能有何种感受。

因此，除却这段插叙本身或许算得上致歉之外，我将毫不致歉地用那些在我的记忆殿堂中鸣响着往昔最甜美回声的名字，来称呼我青年时代所有更亲密、更珍爱的同伴；因为在任何不愿摒弃外部尘俗生活行异步伐之外的一切乐章的心灵中，与这些名字同频共振的心弦都绝不能长久停止悸动。

我说过，当萨姆赶上我时，我再次充满了漫游的狂热。我恳求他与我同行，用最绚烂的色彩描绘着我所规划的这场庞大旅行将为他对宏伟宇宙（Kosmos）的认知增添何等宝藏。他答应至少充当几百英里的同行旅伴（compagnon de voyage），于是我们当即便启程出发。

===Original===

Our way led through a broad meadow, at that season beautifully green, and before my gaze it grew into a tremendous Asiatic plateau thronged with innumerable Tartars. As if assembling for a foray, they rushed past me in mad haste, their oblique eyes snapping with a ferocious light, and plumes of horsehair streaming from their tufted caps. It is not possible to convey to a mind in its ordinary state the effect produced by beholding a field which one has been accustomed to see vacant suddenly bristling with weird and foreign forms, which by perfect distinctness of outline equal in reality, while they surpass in impressiveness the most usual objects of daily sight.
Sam was a man unexcelled by any of his age that I have ever met for the breadth of his historic, geographical, and political knowledge. Mention a fact in the Saracen annals, and straightway he would give you its date, and run its parallel of chronological latitude through all the empires and dynastics of the world. The name of the most inconsiderable place suggested to him everything of note that had ever been transacted in its neighborhood, and on the factious efforts of an Athenian demagogue he would build you in an instant the intricate fabric of all the coups d’état, revolutions, and strokes of diplomacy up to the present day. It is not to be wondered at, such being the case, that some incongruous remark of my own, which confounded two utterly distinct tribes of Tartary, should grate on his historic taste to such a degree as to force from him a mild correction.
“It is impossible,” said Sam, “that the tribe of which you speak should occupy this territory through whose boundaries you inform me we are traveling.”
The instantaneous thrill of pain which this slight contradiction darted through me can not be imagined by anyone who does not know the intense sensitiveness of the hashish state. In a tone of deepest reproach I said, “Alas! my friends, I see you do not sympathize with me. Let us travel apart.”
So saying, I wandered from his side and walked alone, feeling hurt in the very centre of my pride and self-respect. But Sam, who now saw that he must humor my hallucination, followed me, and appeasing my indignation upon the delicate subject of the Ukraine Tartars, took my arm, and we walked together as before.

===Chinese===

我们的前路穿过一片开阔的草地，在此时节显得格外翠绿，在我的凝望中，它膨胀为一片浩瀚的亚洲大高原，密布着数不清的鞑靼人（Tartars）。宛如正在集结准备突袭，他们以疯狂的速度从我身旁呼啸而过，倾斜的眼眸中闪烁着凶残的光芒，马尾饰羽从他们缀有缨穗的皮帽上飘扬而出。对于处于寻常状态下的心智而言，根本无法传达目睹一片平日司空见惯的空旷原野突然布满光怪陆离的异国形体时所产生的震撼效果——这些形体凭借轮廓的极致清晰，在真实感上堪比、而在动人心魄的感染力上则远胜于日常所见的最寻常景物。

萨姆历史、地理与政治知识的广博，在我所遇见的同龄人中无人能出其右。只要提及撒拉逊历史的一桩史实，他便能立即报出年份，并在世界上所有帝国与王朝的历史长河中划出相应的年代对照。最微不足道的地名，都能让他联想起该地附近曾发生过的所有瞩目事件；根据一位雅典煽动家的派系斗争行径，他能瞬间为你梳理出直至今日的所有政变、革命与外交手腕的复杂脉络。鉴于此种情形，毫不奇怪，当我自己一句前后矛盾的话将两个截然不同的鞑靼部落混为一谈时，竟如此刺痛了他对历史的考究品味，以至于迫使他温和地出言纠正。

“绝无可能，”萨姆说，“你所说的那个部落，不可能占据你口中我们正在穿行的这片疆土。”

这句轻微的反驳瞬间刺穿我心头的剧烈痛楚，任何不了解大麻状态极度过敏特质的人都无法想象。我用最深切的责备口吻说道：“唉！我的朋友，我看出你并不理解我。让我们各奔东西吧。”

言毕，我脱离了他的身旁独自前行，感到自尊与傲骨的核心受到了沉重创伤。然而萨姆此时意识到必须顺应我的幻觉，便跟了上来，在乌克兰鞑靼人这一微妙课题上平息了我的愤慨，重新挽起我的手臂，我们宛如先前一样并肩前行。

===Original===

With all the delicious ecstasy of a traveler who looks for the first time upon the gorgeous piles of medieval architecture, I saw far in the distant east a palace rise sublimely above its emerald terraces. We walked for hours and through leagues, yet it grew no nearer, and I enjoyed the luxury of anticipation indefinitely prolonged, yet growing sweeter by delay. The wind came to me freighted with spicy odors; it whispered of dalliance with citron blossoms, and reeled in playful circles; new-flown from its deep draught among the vines of Muscat. In my ears it sang promises of immortal youth, and added its own wings to my already superhuman lightness.
What mattered it that my far-off battlements were the walls of college, my mighty plain a field, and my wind of balm but an ordinary sunset breeze? To me all joys were real﻿—yes, even with a reality which utterly surpasses the hardest facts of the ordinary world.
Hashish is indeed an accursed drug, and the soul at last pays a most bitter price for all its ecstasies; moreover, the use of it is not the proper means of gaining any insight, yet who shall say that at that season of exaltation I did not know things as they are more truly than ever in the ordinary state? Let us not assert that the half-careless and uninterested way in which we generally look on nature is the normal mode of the soul’s power of vision. There is a fathomless meaning, an intensity of delight in all our surroundings, which our eyes must be unsealed to see. In the jubilance of hashish, we have only arrived by an improper pathway at the secret of that infinity of beauty which shall be beheld in heaven and earth when the veil of the corporeal drops off, and we know as we are known. Then from the muddy waters of our life, defiled by the centuries of degeneracy through which they have flowed, we shall ascend to the old-time original fount, and grow rapturous with its apocalyptic draught.
But for this reflection I had never abandoned hashish. Yet through all the long agonies which attended its abjurement, I consoled myself with the knowledge that the infinite glories of the past should beam on me again. I had caught a glimpse through the chinks of my earthly prison of the immeasurable sky which should one day overarch me with an unconceived sublimity of view, and resound in my ear with unutterable music.

===Chinese===

带着初次凝望中世纪建筑瑰丽华宇的行者的全部美妙狂喜，我望见远在遥远的东方，一座宫殿庄严地耸立在翡翠般的梯级平台之上。我们漫步了数个钟头，穿过了数里格之遥，那宫殿却未见丝毫临近，而我则享受着被无限延长的期盼的奢华，这期盼反因延迟而愈显甘甜。微风向我吹拂而来，满载着香料的芬芳；它轻声诉说着与香橼花戏谑缠绵的私语，嬉闹着打着回旋；它方才在马斯喀特（Muscat）的葡萄藤丛中痛饮饱醉，振翼初飞。它在我耳畔歌唱着永驻青春的誓言，并为我本已超凡脱俗的轻灵平添了它自身的羽翼。

哪怕我远方的城堞不过是大学的围墙，我广袤的大平原不过是一片农田，我的温煦芳风不过是寻常的晚霞微风，那又何妨？于我而言，所有的欢愉皆真实不虚——是的，甚至带有一种彻底超越寻常世界最冰冷硬邦事实的真实感。

大麻确实是一种该受诅咒的药物，灵魂最终要为其全部狂喜付出最惨痛的代价；况且，使用它绝非获取任何洞见的合宜途径；然而，谁又能断言在那亢奋的时节里，我对事物本质的认识没有比在寻常状态下更为真切呢？我们切莫断言，平日里以漫不经心、毫无兴致的方式打量自然，便是灵魂视界机能的常态模式。在我们身周的一切事物中，蕴藏着深不可测的意义与炽烈的欢愉，唯有启封双眼方能得见。在大麻的欢腾中，我们不过是以不合宜的蹊径，窥破了当天国的肉身面纱脱落、当我们得以如被全知般洞悉一切时天地间所展现的那无尽至美的奥秘。届时，我们将从流经数百年堕落而受污染的生活泥泞浊流中，攀升至昔日太古的原初清泉，因痛饮其天启琼浆而心醉神迷。

若非因为这一层思索，我本绝不会戒绝大麻。然而在伴随戒断而来的漫长痛苦中，我因确信往昔无尽的荣光终将再次照耀于我而深感慰藉。透过尘世牢狱的缝隙，我曾瞥见那无垠的天宇——终有一日，它将以未曾设想的崇高景观覆庇我头顶，并在我耳畔回荡着不可名状的仙乐。

===Original===

Then I stayed myself upon the hope, and grew into calm endurance.
We may depend upon it, we have not read the world within or the world without. Some mystic wind, like that of hashish, now and then just flutters the leaves of those shut books as it passes by, and the gleam of the divine characters for an instant ravishes us. As from children too young to bear them, they are kept against that day when, grown into perfect men, the props, and helps, and screens of the earthly shall be removed from us, and “the books shall be opened.”
Presently we reached the doors of college. I do not remember whether I have yet mentioned that in the hashish state an occasional awaking occurs, perhaps as often as twice in an hour (though I have no way of judging accurately, from the singular properties of the hashish time), when the mind returns for an exceedingly brief space to perfect consciousness, and views all objects in their familiar light. Such an awaking occurred to me as we drew near the steps of the building, and I took advantage of it to request Sam that he would conduct me to the room of another friend of mine, if he were unable to remain longer with me himself. He answered that he was obliged to leave me, and accordingly led me to the place I had mentioned. The hashish fantasia having returned directly after I had made my request, I might never have been able to find it alone.
Repeatedly have I wandered past doors and houses which, in my ordinary condition, were as well known as my own, and have at last given up the search for them in utter hopelessness, recognizing not the faintest familiar trace in their aspect. Certainly, a hashish eater should never be alone.
I found Sidney in his room: in his charge Sam left me, after apprising him of my state, and I easily persuaded him to go with me on my travels. Back of the buildings a very large domain of woods and fields extends toward the east. From the door of one of the entries a continuous path leads to the further extent of these grounds, and into this path we struck. The evening shadows were deepening, yet the woods had not yet become so sombre as to wear that terrible air of mystery which, among them, in my after hashish-life, oppressed me to an unbearable degree, even in the daylight.

===Chinese===

于是我寄托于希望，化作从容坚忍。

我们大可确信：我们未曾读懂内在的世界，亦未曾读懂外在的世界。某种神秘的风，宛如大麻之风，偶尔在拂过时吹动那些合拢之书的纸页，神圣字符的光华在刹那间令我们心驰神往。正如防备孩童年幼不胜其负一样，它们被珍藏起来，以待那一日的到来——届时，当我们成长为完全的人，尘世的支架、拐杖与屏障皆将从我们身旁撤去，“案卷必将展开”。

不久我们来到了大学校门前。我不记得自己是否曾提及过：在大麻状态下，偶尔会出现清醒的间歇，或许一小时有两次之多（尽管由于大麻时间的奇特属性，我无法准确判定），届时心智会在极短暂的瞬间恢复完全清醒，以熟悉的目光看待一切物体。当我们走近主楼台阶时，我便经历了这样一次清醒，我趁机恳请萨姆，若他自己无法再继续陪我，能否将我引至另一位朋友的房间。他回答说他不得不离开我，于是便依言将我引向了我提及的处所。大麻幻境画卷在我提出请求后立即卷土重来，若让我独自一人，我恐怕绝无可能找到那里。

不知有多少次，我曾在寻常状态下同自家一样熟悉的门扉与房舍前走过，却在它们的外貌中辨认不出哪怕一丝熟悉的痕迹，最终在彻底的绝望中放弃了搜寻。毫无疑问，食大麻者绝不应当独处。

我在悉尼（Sidney）的房间里找到了他：在向他说明了我的状态后，萨姆将我托付于他照看，而我轻而易举地便说服了他同我一起踏上漫游。在楼房后方，一大片林地与田野向东延伸。从一处入口的门前，有一条连贯的小径通往这些场地的深处，我们便步入了这条小径。暮色渐浓，林木尚未变得如此阴森以至于带上那可怕的神秘气息——在此后的大麻生涯中，置身林间，即便在光天化日之下，那气息亦曾令我感到难以承受的压抑。

===Original===

Our way skirted the banks of a little stream, which, tinkling over its rocky bed, makes music through all those shades from boundary to boundary. Coming to a convenient place, we crossed it on broad stepping-stones a pebble’s throw from a low waterfall, which, higher up the bed, was now swollen by recent rains. An instantaneous dart of exultation shot through me. Could it be possible? Yes, true, beyond doubt! I clapped my hands and cried, “The Nile! the Nile! the eternal Nile!” Lo, now I was Bruce, and beside me walked Clapperton. “Companion of my journey,” I exclaimed, “see you yonder cataract? Above it lie the sources. Out of that gleaming chasm which you behold toward the east, this mystery-veiled river has poured his floods since God first awakened the years. I drink in the ecstasy of his material fount now for the second time. Through lonely pilgrimages I toiled, footsore, in the desert; my life hung, many a night of sleeplessness and many a day of famine, upon the mood of ferocious men; I did all things, I suffered all things; and one day, at even, the sources broke upon me. Oh, that unshared view was glory enough for a lifetime!” “But why,” asked Clapperton, “has the world never known this discovery of yours? In all my wanderings (and, as you are aware, they have been only exceeded by your own), I have never heard of your visit to this fountain before.”
“I died in the desert on my way homeward. As I felt the unmistakable signs of death come upon me, I gathered strength to trace upon a small piece of paper a few words, simply stating the fact of the discovery, and the bearings of the sources. This I committed to my guide, extorting from him a reluctant promise never to part with it until he had carried it to my friends at Alexandria.”
“Why reluctant?”
“Because he declared that it was sacrilege to unveil the forehead of the Nile, and that he dreaded some fearful recompense for his impiety.”
“Where is that paper now? Did he fulfill his promise?”
“No. He carried the writing as far as Alexandria, and there, being overcome by the terrors of his superstition, burned it, and forever deprived me of the triumph of my labors. Yet with you, Clapperton﻿—you, who so well know my toils﻿—I rejoice as if the world were applauding me. Glory, glory in the highest, that I behold again﻿—that I behold with *you*﻿—the Nile, the eternal Nile!”
My eyes ran tears of ecstasy.

===Chinese===

我们的前路沿着一条小溪的岸边蜿蜒，溪水在石质河床上叮咚流淌，在整片绿荫的每一处边界之间奏响乐章。来到一处便宜之地，我们在离一座低矮瀑布仅有一掷石子之遥的地方，踩着宽阔的踏脚石过溪；那座位于河床更高处的瀑布，此刻因近期的降雨而水量丰沛。一阵瞬间的狂喜射穿了我的心胸。难道可能吗？是的，千真万确，毋庸置疑！我拍手高呼：“尼罗河！尼罗河！永恒的尼罗河！”看哪，此时我是布鲁斯（Bruce），而在我身旁漫步的是克拉珀顿（Clapperton）。“我的旅伴啊，”我惊呼道，“你看见那边的瀑布了吗？源头就在其上方。从你朝东所见的那道闪烁峡谷中，这条笼罩着神秘面纱的大河，自上帝最初唤醒岁月起便倾泻着它的洪流。如今我平生第二次痛饮其物质泉眼的狂喜。在孤寂的朝圣中，我步履维艰、脚底生疮地跋涉在沙漠之中；多少个不眠之夜，多少个饥馑之日，我的性命全系于凶暴之徒的一念之间；我历经万事，饱受万难；终于在某天的黄昏，源头豁然展现在我眼前。噢，那未与旁人共享的一瞥，足可成为一生的荣耀！”“可是，”克拉珀顿问道，“世人为何从未得知你的这一发现？在我的全部漫游中（如你所知，我的漫游仅次于你一人），我此前从未听说过你曾造访过这处源泉。”

“我在折返回家的沙漠途中死去了。当我感到死亡确凿无疑的征兆降临在身上时，我拼尽力气在一小片纸条上草草写下几行字，简略陈述了发现的事实以及源头的方位。我将纸条托付给我的向导，强令他勉强应允：在将它送到我在亚历山大港（Alexandria）的朋友手中之前，绝不离身。”

“为何勉强？”

“因为他断言揭开尼罗河的前额乃是大逆不道的亵渎之举，他惧怕因自己的不敬而招致某种可怕的报应。”

“那张纸如今在何处？他履行了诺言吗？”

“没有。他把字条带到了亚历山大港，在那里，因克服不了迷信带来的恐惧，他将字条付之一炬，永远剥夺了我辛劳成果的凯旋。然而同你在一起，克拉珀顿——你深知我的艰辛——我欣喜若狂，宛如全世界都在为我喝彩。至高无上的荣耀啊，我竟然能够再次目睹——同*你*一起目睹——尼罗河，永恒的尼罗河！”
我的眼中淌下了狂喜的泪水。

===Original===

I clasped Clapperton to my bosom in speechless joy. I heard the river in its upper caverns hymning such invitations as float down to the seer, entranced, from the lips of angels. Bruce revisiting earth felt such exultation as can only be excelled by that of Bruce first freed from earth.
Leaving the banks of the Nile, we struck deeper into the dense shade of pines and chestnuts, which, to my sense, were spice-trees of the African wilderness. On a stile over which our way led sat two students repeating Shakespeare to each other. To avoid their beholding my rejoicing, Sid gently took me into another path, yet we came near enough to hear one sentence:

> “With this, farewell; I’m on my way to Padua.”

===Chinese===

我在无言的喜悦中将克拉珀顿拥入怀中。我听见上游洞穴中的河流吟唱着邀请，恰如自天使唇齿间飘落至出神入定先知耳畔的仙乐。布鲁斯重返人间所体会的狂喜，唯有布鲁斯初次脱离尘世时的欣喜方能超越。

离开尼罗河畔，我们深入到松树与板栗树的浓密绿荫深处，在我的感知中，它们已然化作了非洲荒野中的香料树。在我们必经之路的一处阶梯式梯蹬（stile）上，坐着两名学生，正彼此对背莎士比亚戏剧的台词。为了避免他们看到我的狂喜，悉尼轻轻地将我引向另一条小径，然而我们靠得足够近，听见了一句话：

> “就此告别；我正动身前往帕多瓦。”

===Original===

(Not exactly Shakespeare, but they meant it to be, and I was not in a mood to cavil.)
In an instant, like the shifting of a scene, all the thoughts and images of Africa vanished. Italy, the glad, the sunny, took its place, and the wood grew dense with palaces and fountains. In a broad piazza we sauntered up and down, transfused with a dreamy summer languor, or strolled from portico to portico, on all sides surrounded by the most beautiful creations of Art.
At first I had a dim conception of the unreality of this vision, for I saw its groundwork in certain material things, remembered as once existing in other forms. For instance, I sometimes perceived the development of an arch in its transition state from two curved branches which locked over us, and now and then a new column grew up gradually from the vacant light-spaces between two trunks of trees. But in a very short time, of course, much shorter than I supposed, every suspicion of the imaginary utterly vanished from my mind, and I no more doubted our being in some fair Italian city than I doubted my own existence.
The effect of the hashish increased, as it always does, with the excitement of the visions and the exercise of walking. I began to be lifted into that tremendous pride which is so often a characteristic of the fantasia. My powers became superhuman; my knowledge covered the universe; my scope of sight was infinite. I was invested with a grand mission to humanity, and slowly it dawned upon me that I was the Christ, come in the power and radiance of his millennial descent, and bearing to the world the restoration of perfect peace. I spoke, and it was done: with a single sentence I regenerated the Creation. A smile of exultation beamed from the awakened earth. I could hear her low music of rejoicing as she perceived that the fullness of the times with which, for centuries, she had travailed in woe, had at length been brought forth. All men once more lived in love to God and their neighbor, and, secure in an eternal compact, began marching on harmoniously to the sublime end of spiritual greatness.

===Chinese===

（倒不完全算是莎士比亚的原话，但他们本意如此，而我也无心挑剔。）
顷刻间，如同换景一般，关于非洲的所有思绪与意象荡然无存。欢愉晴朗的意大利取而代之，林木之间密布起座座宫殿与喷泉。在一座宽阔的广场（piazza）上，我们踱步徜徉，沉浸在如梦如幻的夏日慵懒之中，或在门廊之间穿梭漫步，四面皆被艺术最瑰丽的造物所环绕。

起初，我对这幅幻象的虚幻性尚有一丝模糊的认知，因为我能看出它立足于某些物质实体之上，并依稀记得它们曾以其他形态存在。譬如，有时我能察觉到一座拱门正处于由我们头顶交错的两根弯曲树枝过渡演变的阶段；偶尔，一座新的圆柱正从两根树干之间的空旷光斑间逐渐拔地而起。但在极短的时间内——当然，比我预想的要短得多——对虚构的一切怀疑彻底从我脑海中烟消云散，我不再怀疑我们正身处某座秀美的意大利城市，恰如不再怀疑我自身的存在。

大麻的药效随着幻象带来的亢奋与步行的运动而剧增，向来如此。我开始被提升到那庞大的骄傲之中，而这正是大麻幻境屡见不鲜的特质。我的力量变得超凡脱俗；我的学识涵盖整个宇宙；我的视界无边无际。我被赋予了一项对人类的伟大救赎使命，而我逐渐领悟到：我便是基督，在千禧年降临的权能与荣光中降世，为世间带来完美和平的复兴。我一声令下，事便成了：只凭一句话，我便重造了整个受造界。苏醒的大地绽放出欣喜若狂的微笑。我能听见她欢庆的低徊乐音，因为她领悟到，数个世纪以来她在苦难中所阵痛期盼的时机圆满，终于诞生了。所有人再次生活在对上帝与邻舍的博爱之中，在永恒盟约的庇护下，和谐地迈向属灵伟大的崇高终局。

===Original===

The nature of all beasts grew mild; the satyr walked down from his mountain fastness, and led his young fearlessly into the presence of his old foe, the leopard; the kite and the dove imped their wings upon the same branch; out of the depths of the jungle the tiger stepped forth and gently drew near to fawn upon his king. The terrible lustre of his eyes was dissolved into the serene light of love, and as I caressed his spotted hide, he returned the kindness with a thankful purr.
My mission being accomplished, we passed on. Returning to the college, a most singular phenomenon presented itself. The faces of all that I met were metamorphosed into appearances which symbolized some inner attribute, or some speciality of manners and habits. One of my friends was an admirable whist-player, noted for his accurate observance and employment of the times and seasons for returning leads, finessing, and crying privilege. His face was changed to a fan-like display of cards, which winked at me with a quiet and balmy air of exultation, as in the consciousness of being

> “Quite irresistible,

Like a man with eight trumps in his hand at a whist-table.”

===Chinese===

百兽的天性皆变得温驯和善；萨堤尔走下险峻的高山城堡，无所畏惧地领着幼崽来到宿敌猎豹的面前；鸢与鸽在同一根树枝上梳理羽翼；猛虎从丛林深处迈步而出，温柔走近，向它的君王曲意逢迎。它双目中可怖的凶光消解为宁静的慈爱光芒；当我抚摸它斑斓的毛皮时，它以感激的呼噜声回报我的仁爱。

使命既已告成，我们继续前行。回到学院后，一桩最奇异的现象呈现了出来。我遇见的每一个人的面孔，皆蜕变为象征其某种内在特质、或举止习性之特异之处的外貌。我的一位朋友是极为出色的惠斯特桥牌手，以精准洞察并运用回牌、飞牌与叫牌的时机而闻名。他的面孔化作了一排扇形展开的纸牌，带着一种安详温煦的狂喜神情向我眨眼，宛如自知

> “无可匹敌，

宛如惠斯特牌桌上手握八张王牌的豪客。”

===Original===

Another, famous for his studently habits, a great reader, and fond of research, looked at me for a moment, and his visage immediately turned into a bookcase bristling with encyclopedias. I stretched forth my hand to take one from its shelf, and, by a sudden outcry, became aware that I had performed that amiable office known among mortals as pulling one’s nose.
But this vision of the ludicrous was soon dissipated by the return of the former ecstasy of pride. Now braced for its exaltation by the few moments of jocose refreshment, I towered into all the sublimity of self-adulation. Pacing the floor, which, for my display, had been changed into that of the Senate, as Webster revivified, I rolled a thundercloud of colossal argument over the head of a mythical opponent, and brought all time to the witness-stand with testimony against the direful results of some intemperate measure.
And now, the hallucination changing, I was exhaustlessly rich, and as exhaustlessly benevolent. Through long avenues I walked between kneeling files of poor, and scattered handfuls of gold into their bosoms. “Be comfortable, be opulent, be luxurious,” I cried; and as the metallic rain dripped from my thrilling fingers, again the plaudits of my march poured in upon me, and the famine-stricken shouted, “Our savior!” I rejoiced in the measureless pride of bounteousness.
Awaking on the morrow after a succession of vague and delicious dreams, I had not yet returned to the perfectly natural state. I now began to experience a law of hashish which developed its effects more and more through all the future months of its use. With the progress of the hashish life, the effect of every successive indulgence grows more perduring until the hitherto isolated experiences become tangent to each other; then the links of the delirium intersect, and at last so blend that the chain has become a continuous band, now resting with joyous lightness as a chaplet, and now mightily pressing in upon the soul like the glowing hoop of iron which holds martyrs to the stake. The final months of this spellbound existence, be it terminated by mental annihilation or by a return into the quiet and mingled facts of humanity, are passed in one unbroken yet checkered dream.
In the morning the ludicrous side of the hashish sphere alone was turned toward me.

===Chinese===

另一位朋友以笃学嗜书著称，博览群书且酷爱探究；他看了我一眼，面孔随即化作了一座塞满百科全书的连排书架。我伸手打算从架上抽出一本来，却因一声骤然的惊叫而意识到，自己方才干了一件凡俗尘世中被称作揪人鼻子的亲昵勾当。

然而，这出滑稽的幻象很快便被先前的傲慢狂喜的卷土重来所驱散。由于经历了方才片刻诙谐的休整，此刻我重整旗鼓迎向那亢奋，高高耸立入自负的崇高绝顶之中。我踱步于为我的展示而化作参议院大厅的地板上，宛如韦伯斯特（Webster）重生，向着一位虚构对手的头顶倾泻下浩瀚论辩的雷云风暴，并将千秋岁月皆召至证人席上，作证控诉某项过激举措的灾难性后果。

此时，幻觉再次转换，我变得拥有取之不尽的财富，并怀有同样取之不尽的仁慈。穿过漫长的林荫大道，我漫步在两旁长跪不起的穷苦人群之间，将一把把黄金抛洒在他们的怀中。“安乐吧，富足吧，享乐吧！”我高呼道；当金属的雨滴从我激动的指尖滚落时，为我行军喝彩的掌声再次如潮水般向我涌来，饥民们高呼着：“我们的救主！”我沉浸在慷慨解囊的无尽自豪之中。

次日在经历了一连串模糊甜美的梦境后醒来，我尚未完全重返自然状态。我如今开始体会到大麻的一条规律，在未来吸食它的数月里，这条规律的作用愈发显著。随着大麻生涯的推进，每一次服食的药效都变得愈发持久，直到先前各自孤立的体验彼此相切相交；随后谵妄的环扣相互交织，最终完全交融，使得整条锁链化为一条连续不断的纽带：时而如花冠般以喜悦的轻盈覆在心头，时而如将殉道者锁在火刑柱上的白热铁箍般狠狠压迫着灵魂。这段被施了魔法的生存状态的收官数月——无论它是以精神的彻底湮灭而告终，还是以重返凡尘宁静交织的现实而落幕——皆是在一场连绵不绝却斑驳错杂的幻梦中度过的。

清晨时分，唯有大麻世界的滑稽一面展现在我的眼前。

===Original===

I was whirled through the progress of an infinite number of strange transmutations. Now, as a powerful saw in some mill of a northern lumber region, I darted up and down at the imperative instigation of an overshot wheel, and on either side of me the planks flew off in the utmost completeness of manufacture. Now changed to a bottle of soda-water, I ran hither and thither with intricate and rapid involutions, pursued by an army of publicans, who, with awl in hand, were trying to break the wires which kept in my vital effervescence. Weak with laughter, for I was strangely reckless of the peril which my life sustained, I sat down to rest, having distanced the whole troop of my persecutors. Suddenly the sentiment of an intense mortification overcame me. “Is it possible,” I soliloquized, “that thou, the descendant of an ancient and glorious line, canst be so utterly dishonored as to merge thy being in one of society’s grossest and basest potables? Child worthy of a better destiny, I will implore the gods for thee, that in their condescension they may elevate thee to some more spiritual essence.” No sooner said than done. My neck grew longer, my head was night-capped with snowy kid, ethereal odors of delight streamed through my brain, and, exultant with apotheosis, I beheld by patent of nobility stamped on my crystal breast in these golden characters:

> **Eau de Cologne.

Jean Maria de Farina.**

===Chinese===

我被卷入了一连串数不清的奇特变形过程。时而，作为北方伐木区某家锯木厂里的一把强劲钢锯，我在上冲式水轮的号令驱动下上下飞窜，两侧加工完工的木板完好整齐地飞落开去。时而化作一瓶苏打水，我以复杂而迅疾的回旋忽东忽西地奔逃，身后追赶着一队酒馆掌柜，他们手持锥子，企图挑断禁锢我鲜活气泡的铁丝。我笑得浑身发软——因为我竟奇特地对生命所承受的危机毫不在意——在将那群迫害者远远甩开之后，我坐下歇息。突然，一种极度屈辱的情绪攫住了我。“难道说，”我自言自语道，“你作为古老而荣耀血统的后裔，竟能堕落到如此毫无尊严的地步，将自身的存在融入市井间最粗俗下贱的饮品之中吗？本配享有更崇高宿命的孩子啊，我将为你向诸神祈求，愿诸神俯允恩典，将你提升至某种更具灵性的纯粹精魄。”言出必行。我的颈项拉长，头顶罩上了雪白的山羊皮睡帽，空灵愉悦的幽香流淌穿透我的脑际；在封神化圣的狂喜中，我看见我的水晶胸膛上印着彰显贵族血统特许状的金色大字：

> 科隆香水（Eau de Cologne）。

让·玛利亚·德·法里纳（Jean Maria de Farina）。

===Original===

A lordly hippopotamus, I wandered in from the wilderness, and with my forefoot knocked at the door of a friend of mine celebrated for wasting the midnight burning-fluid in the pursuit of classical and mathematical researches. “Tidings!” I cried; “tidings from the interior of Africa!” With a look of astonishment and half terror, for he had never seen me in the hashish state before, the lover of books opened unto me, and I passed in.
An unceremonious hippopotamus, I sat down, with the most incongruous disregard of the breadth of beam proper to my species, in the nearest chair, without explanation or apology. A poetical hippopotamus, I soared into a sublime description of equatorial crags, medio-terrene lakes, and marshy jungles. I expatiated upon the delights of an Ethiopian existence; I grew rapturous over the remembered ecstasy of mud-baths and lunches upon succulent lotus-stems by riversides where Nature kept free restaurant for pachydermatous gentlemen forever.
Ed was a man of strong social impulses, most kindly heart, and high appreciation of the beautiful. Yet at that moment, being ensconced in a tremendous munition of tomes, and supplied with stores of reading which might sustain the most protracted siege, pleaded preoccupation, and begged me to defer my lecture on the African *far niente*. I consented, with some indignation, however, at his lack of taste. I opened the door to leave his room for the sake of finding a more respectful auditor, when lo! to my shame, I had altogether mistaken my species, for I was the tallest giraffe that ever dallied amorously with a palm-bud. Abasing my exalted head to suit the dimensions of the door, I passed out, and was again restored to the human semblance.

===Chinese===

作为一只威严高傲的河马，我自荒野漫游而来，用前足叩响了我的一位朋友的房门，他因通宵达旦耗费燃油潜心钻研古典与数学著称。“消息！”我大喊道，“来自非洲内陆的最新消息！”带着惊诧与半带恐惧的神情——因为他此前从未见过我处于大麻状态下的模样——这位书痴为我打开了门，我大摇大摆地迈了进去。

作为一只不拘小节的河马，我完全不顾自身物种应有的庞大身形，既无解释亦无致歉，径直坐进了离得最近的一把椅子里。作为一只充满诗意的河马，我高谈阔论，对赤道绝壁、内陆湖泊与沼泽丛林展开了一番崇高的描摹。我极尽铺陈着非洲生活的诸般妙处；回想起在河畔泥浆浴中打滚以及享用鲜美多汁荷茎午餐的狂喜，我不禁陶醉其中——在那些河畔，大自然永远为厚皮类绅士们开设着免费餐馆。

埃德（Ed）是个社交欲望强烈、宅心仁厚且对至美极富鉴赏力的人。然而在那一刻，他正深陷于大批厚重典籍的坚固工事之中，身旁的阅读储备足以支撑最持久的围困；他推托自己手头正忙，央求我推迟发表关于非洲悠闲无为（*far niente*）的宏论。我同意了，但对他的不识抬举深感愤慨。我推开门离开他的房间，打算去寻找一位更懂尊重的听众；正在此时，看哪！令我羞愧的是，我完全弄错了自身的物种：我分明是一头平生曾与棕榈花蕾含情调笑过的最高耸的长颈鹿。我顺从房门的尺寸低下我高高耸立的头颅，迈步跨出门外，并重新恢复了人形。

## VIII *Vos Non Vobis*﻿—Wherein the Pythagorean Is a Bystander / 第八章 尔曹非为己：毕达哥拉斯门徒作为旁观者
===Original===

The judgment that must be passed upon the hashish life in retrospect is widely different from the one which I formed during its progress. Now the drug, with all its revelation of interior mysteries, its glimpses of supernatural beauty and sublimity, appears as the very witch-plant of hell, the weed of madness. At the time of its daily use, I forgave it for all its pangs, for its cruel exercise of authority, its resistless fascination, and its usurpation of the place of all other excitement, at the intercession of the divine forms which it created for my soul, and which, though growing rarer and rarer, when they were present retained their glory until the last. Moreover, through many ecstasies and many pains, I still supposed that I was only making experiments, and that, too, in the most wonderful field of mind which could be opened for investigation, and with an agent so deluding in its influence that the soul only became aware that the strength of a giant was needed to escape when its locks were shorn.
In accordance with these facts, I did not suppose that I was imperiling any friend of mine by giving him an opportunity to make the same experiment which he beheld producing in me phenomena so astonishing to a mind in love with research. Several of my intimate associates applied to me for the means of experimentally gratifying their curiosity upon the subject, and to some of them, as favorable opportunities presented themselves, I administered hashish, remaining by their side during the progress of the effects. In no other experience can difference of temperament, physical and mental, produce such varieties of phenomena; nowhere can we attain so well defined an idea of this difference. I shall, therefore, devote this chapter to the relation of some of the more remarkable of these cases.
Upon William N﻿—﻿— hashish produced none of the effects characteristic of fantasia. There was no hallucination, no volitancy of unusual images before the eye when closed. Circulation, however, grew to a surprising fullness and rapidity, accompanied by the same introversion of faculties and clear perception of all physical processes which startled me in my first experiment upon myself.

===Chinese===

回首往事，对大麻生涯所必须作出的评判，同我身处其中时所形成的观念大相径庭。如今，这种药物连同它对内在奥秘的一切启示、对超自然之美与崇高的惊鸿一瞥，在我眼中无异于地狱的妖草、令人发狂的毒株。而在当年日常服食的岁月里，由于它为我的灵魂创造了神圣的形体——这些形体尽管日益罕见，但只要出现便至死保留着它们的荣光——在它们的代求之下，我宽恕了大麻带来的一切剧痛、对其权威的残酷施展、其无可抗拒的魔力，以及它对所有其他兴奋手段地位的篡夺。更有甚者，在历经诸多狂喜与痛苦之中，我依然误以为自己仅仅是在做实验，且置身于探究所能开辟的最奇妙的心智领域，借助一种药效如此善于惑人的媒介——以至于唯有当发丝被剪断时，灵魂才猛然醒悟：要挣脱桎梏需要巨人之力。

鉴于这些事实，我当时并不认为给朋友提供机会、让他们尝试在热衷探究的心智看来如此惊人的一模一样的实验，会让他们身陷险境。我的几位密友曾向我求取药物，以期借由亲身体验满足自己对这一课题的好奇心；逢有合宜的时机，我便给其中几位服下大麻，并在药力发作的过程中守在他们身旁。在任何其他体验中，肉体与精神体质的差异皆无法催生出如此千差万别的现象；在任何其他领域，我们亦无法对这种差异获得如此清晰明确的认识。因此，我将以此章专门记述其中几个最为引人注目的案例。

在威廉·N（William N——）身上，大麻未曾引发任何幻境画卷特有的反应。没有幻觉，阖上双眼时眼前亦未见任何离奇意象的翩跹飞掠。然而，其血液循环却惊人地充沛而急促，并伴随着与我初次亲身实验时令我惊骇不已的同等心智机能内省，以及对所有肉体运转过程的清晰感知。

===Original===

There was stertorous breathing, dilation of the pupil, and a drooping appearance of the eyelid, followed at last by a comatose state, lasting for hours, out of which it was almost impossible fully to arouse the energies. These symptoms, together with a peculiar rigidity of the muscular system, and inability to measure the precise compass and volume of the voice when speaking, brought the case nearer in resemblance to those recorded by Dr. O’Shaughnessy, of Calcutta, as occurring under his immediate inspection among the natives of India than any I have ever witnessed.
In William N﻿—﻿— I observed, however, one phenomenon which characterizes hashish existence in persons of far different constitutions﻿—the expansion of time and space. Walking with him a distance not exceeding a furlong, I have seen him grow weary and assume a look of hopelessness, which he explained by telling me that he never could traverse the immensity before him. Frequently, also, do I remember his asking to know the time thrice in as many minutes, and when answered, he exclaimed, “Is it possible? I supposed it an hour since I last inquired.” His temperament was a mixture of the phlegmatic and nervous, and he was generally rather unsusceptible to stimulus. I was anxious at the time that he should be favorably affected, since he had been, and afterward was still more so, in an eminent degree, the kindhearted assuager of my sufferings and increaser of my joys in many an experience of hashish. To him I ran, many a time, for companionship in my hashish journeyings, and always found in him full appreciation and sympathy.
I am now glad that he learned none of the fascination of the drug, for Heaven only, and not the hashish-eater in any wise, knows where it will lead him.
One of my friends in college was a man to whom it would have been physically, spiritually, and morally impossible ever to have borne any other name than Bob, the name by which he was called among all his intimates, and which has an air eminently expressive of his nature. Impulsive, enthusiastic in his affections, generous to a fault; excitable, fond of queer researches and romantic ventures, there is no other cognomen which would so typify him as to give more than a shadow image of his constitution﻿—none which would so incarnate him as not to leave some elbow of his inner being sticking out in the improper place.

===Chinese===

他呼吸粗重作鼾，瞳孔放大，眼睑下垂，最终陷入昏迷昏睡状态，长达数小时之久，几乎无法彻底唤醒其生机。这些症状，连同肌肉系统特有的僵直，以及说话时无法准确拿捏声音的音域与音量，比我所目睹过的任何案例，都更接近加尔各答的奥肖内西博士（Dr. O’Shaughnessy）在印度土著中亲自严密观察所记录的病例。

然而，在威廉·N身上，我观察到了一种体质迥异的人在大麻生存状态中所特有的共有现象——时间与空间的膨胀。陪他步行的距离不过一浪（furlong，约二百米），我便见他面露倦容，流露出绝望的神情；他向我解释说，自己绝无可能跨越眼前这片无垠的长途。我还清楚记得，他常常在短短三分钟内三次询问时间，得到答复后惊呼道：“这怎么可能？我还以为距我上次询问已经过去了一个钟头。”他的体质介于粘液质与神经质之间，平日里对刺激物相当迟钝。当时我很渴望他能获得良好的药效体验，因为在大麻的诸多体验中，无论过去还是此后，他都在极大程度上是我痛苦的仁厚抚慰者与欢乐的助兴者。不知有多少次，我奔向他寻求大麻漫游中的陪伴，并总能从他那里获得充分的理解与共情。

如今我深感庆幸他并未领教这种药物的魔力，因为唯有苍天——而食大麻者本人绝无可能——知晓它将把人引向何方。

我在大学里的一位挚友，是一个无论在肉体、精神还是道德层面上，都绝不可能冠以除鲍勃（Bob）之外任何其他名字的人；所有密友都这样称呼他，而这个名字散发着一种极能体现其天性的特质。他冲动鲁莽，情感热烈，慷慨到了过分的程度；易受触动，酷爱古怪的研究与浪漫的冒险；世间再无其他名号能如此传神地刻画他而不至沦为其体质的模糊倒影——没有任何其他名字能如此完美地具象化他，而不致让他内在存在的某个棱角在不适宜的地方唐突地显露出来。

===Original===

It is not surprising that a person of his temperament found much in the hashish condition that was strikingly attractive.
At half past seven in the evening, and consequently after supping instead of before, as I should have preferred, he took twenty-five grains of the drug. This may seem a large bolus to those who are aware that from fifteen grains I frequently got the strongest cannabine effect; but it must be kept in mind that, to secure the full phenomena, a much greater dose is necessary in the first experiment than ever after. Unlike all other stimuli with which I am acquainted, hashish, instead of requiring to be increased in quantity as existence in its use proceeds, demands rather a diminution, seeming to leave, at the return of the natural state (if I may express myself by a rather material analogy), an unconsumed capital of exaltation for the next indulgence to set up business upon.
From the untoward lateness of the hour at which the dose was administered, it was half past ten o’clock before any effects began to show themselves in this case. At that time Bob, and Edward, the reading man, to whose favorable notice I had presented myself under the guise of a hippopotamus, were both seated, together with myself, in a well-lighted room, conversing. Suddenly Bob leaped up from the lounge on which he had been lying, and, with loud peals of laughter, danced wildly over the room. A strange light was in his eyes, and he gesticulated furiously, like a player in pantomime. I was not in the least surprised by these symptoms, for I realized precisely the state of mind through which he was passing; yet my other companion was astonished even to terror with the idea that the experimenter would permanently lose his sanity. Suddenly he stopped dancing, and trembling, as with an undefinable fear, he whispered, “What will become of me?” This question distinctly recalled all the horrible apprehensions of my first experiment; and, though satisfied of the perfect harmlessness of the result, I saw the necessity of steadying the sufferer’s mind upon my own firm assurance of his safety, for the sake of giving him quiet and endurance. I replied, “Trust me, however singularly you may feel, you have not the slightest cause for fear. I have been where you are now, and, upon my honor, guarantee you an unharmed return.

===Chinese===

这样一个秉性的人在大麻状态中觅得诸多引人入胜的绝妙之处，自不足为奇。

晚上七点半——因而是在晚膳之后而非之前，尽管我更倾向于空腹——他服下了二十五格令该药。对于那些知晓我常以十五格令便能获得最强烈大麻效力的人而言，这似乎是一剂大药丸；但必须谨记，若要确保呈现出完整现象，初次实验所需的剂量远比日后多得多。与我所熟知的所有其他刺激物截然不同，随着使用生涯的推进，大麻非但不需要增加用量，反而要求递减；在重返自然状态时（若容我借用某种相当物质化的比喻），它似乎留下了一笔未消耗殆尽的兴奋资本，供下一次的沉湎用作起家之资。

由于服药的时辰不巧偏晚，在此案例中，直到十点半才开始显现药效。当时，鲍勃与爱德华（Edward）——也就是那位爱读书之人，我曾化身河马博取过他的青睐——同我一起坐在一间明亮的房间里攀谈。突然，鲍勃从他一直躺着的卧榻上一跃而起，伴随着阵阵大笑在屋里狂乱起舞。他的双眸闪烁着奇异的光芒，手舞足蹈，宛如哑剧中的演员。我对这些症状丝毫未感惊讶，因为我极其清楚他正经历着何等的心智状态；然而我的另一位同伴却惊愕得近乎恐惧，误以为实验者将永久丧失理智。突然，他止住了舞蹈，瑟瑟发抖着，带着一种难以言状的恐惧窃窃私语道：“我究竟会变成什么样？”这一发问清晰地勾起了我初次实验时经历的一切可怕忧惧；尽管我对结果的全然无害深信不疑，为了给予他安宁与耐力，我意识到必须以我对安全的确凿保证来稳固受术者的心神。我回答道：“相信我，无论你感觉多么古怪，都绝无丝毫害怕的理由。我曾经历过你此刻所处的一切，我以名誉向你担保，你定能安然无恙地归来。

===Original===

No evil will result to you; abandon yourself to the full force of your feelings with perfect confidence that you are in no danger.” Entirely new and unconceived as is the hashish-world, viewed for the first time, the man of greatest natural courage is no more capable of bearing its tremendous realities, unbraced by some such exterior support, than the most feeble woman.
The delirium, now rapidly mounting to its height, made it better that Bob should exert the supernatural activity with which he was endued out of doors, where the air was freer and less constraint was necessary. Clothing my words in as imaginative garb as I was master of, I therefore proposed to him that we should set out on a journey through the wonderful lands of vision. We were soon upon the pavement, he leaping in unbounded delight at the prospect of the grand scenery to come, I ready to humor to the utmost any pleasing fantasy which might possess him; and in the absence of such, or the presence of the contrary, to suggest fine avenues for his thought to followup.
It will of course be perceived that I labor under a great disadvantage from being compelled to relate the progress of subjective states from an objective point of view. My authority for all that I shall give in this case will be my own observation of outward phenomena and my friend’s statement of interior ones, which he gave to me upon returning to consciousness. These latter were expressed with a height of ideality which I feel myself incompetent to give, and gave evidence of as remarkable an inner condition as I have ever known hashish to produce.
On our first leaving the steps of the building, a grand mosque rose upon his vision in the distance, its minarets flaunting with innumerable crescent-emblazoned flags. A mighty plain, covered with no other than a stinted grass, stretched between him and the mosque. Mounted upon Arab horses, with incredible swiftness we sped side by side toward the structure; and I knew when this imagination took place by the answers which he returned me upon my inquiry into the reasons of his prancing as we went. Before we reached the walls, arch and minaret had vanished, and, metamorphosed into an ostrich, he scoured the desert reaches, now utterly void of any human sign.

===Chinese===

不会发生任何不幸；完全放心地任由全部情绪奔涌吧，你绝无任何危险。”初次得见的大麻世界是如此全然崭新而未曾设想，若缺少此类外部的支持依托，即便是生性最勇猛的男子，其承受那磅礴现实的能力也绝不会比最孱弱的妇人强上分毫。

谵妄此刻正迅速攀升至顶点，最好让鲍勃在户外施展他被赋予的超凡活力，那里的空气更为自由，亦无需太多拘束。因此，我尽己所能将言辞披上想象的盛装，向他提议我们动身穿行于神奇的幻象之乡。我们很快踏上了铺石路面，一想到即将得见的壮丽景致，他便纵情狂喜地蹦跳雀跃；我则随时准备竭力顺从迎合可能占据他心头的任何悦人幻想；若无此等幻想，或出现了相反的恐怖念头，我便提示精妙的门径供他的思绪追寻拓展。

读者自然会领会，被迫从客观视角来记述主观状态的发展演进，让我处于极大的劣势之中。在此案例中，我对所提供的一切材料的权威依据，皆来自我自身对外部现象的观察，以及我朋友在恢复清醒后向我陈述的内心经历。后者表达时所达到的崇高意境，令我自己感到笔力不逮，并证明了他所处的内心状态同我所知大麻所能引发的一切同样惊人。

当我们刚迈下大楼台阶时，一座宏伟的清真寺便在他远方的视界中拔地而起，宣礼塔上飘扬着无数饰有新月的旗帜。在与清真寺之间，延伸着一片除了低矮荒草之外别无他物的辽阔平原。我们跨骑在阿拉伯骏马之上，以不可思议的疾速并肩飞驰向那座建筑；当我询问他为何一路雀跃奔跳时，正是从他的回答中得知了这一想象的降临。在我们抵达城墙之前，拱门与宣礼塔已荡然无存；他化身为一只鸵鸟，在如今全无人迹的荒漠旷野中狂奔驰骋。

===Original===

Of this fact also I became aware at the moment from his own lips; for, although in perfect hallucination, the dual existence, as in me, was still perfectly capable of expressing its own states.
It is not one of the least singular facts of hashish that its fantasia almost invariably takes an Oriental form. This can not be explained upon the hypothesis that the experimenter remembers it as an indulgence in use among the people of the East, for at the acme of the delirium there is no consciousness remaining in the mind of its being an unnatural state. The very idea of the drug is utterly forgotten, and present reality shuts out all inquiry into grounds for belief. The only supposition which at all accounts for the fact to my own mind is that the hashish is the antecedent instead of the result of the peculiar characteristics of Oriental mind and manners. The Turk and the Syrian are indeed situated amid surroundings well calculated to stimulate the imaginative nature. A delicious sky, a luxuriant vegetation, and scenery like that of the Bosporous and Damascus are eminently calculated for fascination to dreams and poesy, but then hashish comes bearing an unutterably grander and richer gratification to the same music and odor haunted sense, and makes the highest tone in a harmony already beautiful almost beyond all that earth possesses.
To us, of a mistier atmosphere, yet far more lively perceptions of the very principle of beauty, the drug brings a similar wealth of visions, and, conjoining its influence with a greater scope of sight and strength of thought than the Oriental ever possesses, fills up all deficiencies of exterior sun and landscape by borrowing from the activities of the experimenter.
Eastern architecture, and, in fine, the sum total of Eastern manners, are all the embodiment and symbol of Eastern mind. That mind, or at least its specialty of condition, is very much the product of those stimulants which are in use throughout that portion of the world, and among these hashish holds the regency, as swaying the broadest domain of mind, and most authoritatively ruling all faculties within it. It is therefore the case that, wherever this drug comes into contact with a sensitive organization, the same fruit of supernatural beauty or horror will characterize the visions produced. It is hashish which makes both the Syrian and the Saxon Oriental.

===Chinese===

关于这一事实，我也是在那一刻从他自己的口中获悉的；因为尽管处于完全的幻觉之中，那双重存在却正如在我身上一样，依然完全能够表达自身的所处状态。

大麻最奇异的事实之一，莫过于其幻境画卷几乎无一例外地呈现出东方形式。这绝无法用实验者记起该药是东方人日常嗜好品这一假说来解释，因为在谵妄的顶峰，心智中根本没有留存关于这是一种非自然状态的任何意识。关于药物本身的念头已被彻底遗忘，切近的现实排除了对信仰根据的一切追问。在我心中唯一能合理解释这一事实的假说，便是大麻乃是东方人心智与风俗特质的原因，而非其结果。土耳其人与叙利亚人确实身处极易激发想象天性的环境之中。明媚宜人的苍穹、繁茂丰美的草木，以及宛如博斯普鲁斯海峡（Bosporus）和大马士革（Damascus）般的景致，极易引人沉醉于梦幻与诗篇；然而大麻的降临，为这同样萦绕着音乐与馨香的感官，带来了难以言状的更恢弘、更丰饶的满足，并在本已美得几乎超越尘世所有的和谐乐章中，奏响了至高之音。

对于生活在多雾气候中、却对美之本源拥有更为敏锐感知的我们而言，这种药物带来了同样丰富的幻象，并将其效力与远胜东方人的广阔视野和思维力度相结合，借由实验者自身的思维活力，弥补了外部阳光与风光的全部匮乏。

东方建筑，以及总而言之东方风俗的总和，皆是东方心智的体现与象征。那种心智——或至少其特异的状态——在极大程度上正是该地区普遍使用的各类兴奋剂的产物；而在这些兴奋剂中，大麻统摄全局，因其统御着最广阔的心智疆域，并对其中的一切机能拥有最具权威的支配力。因此，无论这种药物在何处与敏锐的机体相遇，所催生的幻象都将以超自然之美或恐怖的同等硕果为特征。正是大麻，让叙利亚人与撒克逊人皆化作了东方人。

===Original===

That this hypothesis is more than mere vagary, it appears to me, may be proved by numerous parallels running through other nations than the Eastern. It is not mere murky weather, chill winds, and sudden changes of temperature which have built up the walls of reserve in manner and masonry in architecture around the Englishman. His national stimulus is beer, mildly toned by the moderate use of tobacco; his mental result is reticence, solidity, reflectiveness.
Nor does the newness of his country, the peculiarities of his climate, and the demand of his age for rapidity of action alone erect for the American his airy structures, rising with a fungus-vitality from basement to cope in a fortnight, and the pale fence of frankness, which permits insight into all his thoughts. His infants stretch supplicating hands from the cradle toward their father’s tobacco-box; the olive-plants around his table are as regularly fumigated as if they were in a greenhouse; his graybeard uncle (whenever an American takes time to live so long) through all the house continually pipes a fragrant music, to which the remainder of the household do not refuse to dance, and from this most catholic transfusion of nicotine he results in that very anomalous, yet, on the whole, laudable product, our National Man. This man is a singular compound of the visionary and the actual: visionary, because (with other causes, to be sure) his stimulant makes him so; actual, because the necessity of hard work in the New World of intense activity demands it of him. His mind incarnates itself in structures whose decoration and rapidity of finish are accomplished at the risk of safety, permanence, and health, and in manners which caution and reserve only characterize when hard knocks against projecting angles of humanity have taught him the lesson of their needfulness.
His town house is the embodiment of the cigar, as the Briton’s is that of the tankard, and the modes of living of both of them symbolize to a great degree the essence of their several stimulants.
In all civilized nations, the public works of architecture are an exception to this rule, for the design of the pile being more cosmopolitan, national idiosyncrasies are merged in the more comprehensive plan which contains within itself the garnered excellences of all worldly art.

===Chinese===

在我看来，这一假说绝非单纯的空想，只要看看贯穿于东方以外其他民族的诸多对应对照，便能得以佐证。在英国人身周构筑起举止矜持与砖石厚重建筑的高墙的，绝非仅仅是阴霾的天气、凛冽的寒风与骤变的气温。他的民族兴奋剂是啤酒，并伴有适度吸食烟草的温和调剂；其精神产物便是沉稳、内敛与富于反思。

同样，单凭国家的年轻、气候的奇特以及时代对行动迅捷的要求，亦不足以让美国人建起其轻灵的构筑物——在两周之内以真菌般的生命力自地基拔地而起直至封顶——以及那道容许他人窥探其全部心绪的坦率浅白藩篱。他的婴孩从摇篮里伸出恳求的双手抓向父亲的烟盒；餐桌旁的儿女稚苗如置身温室般定期接受烟气熏蒸；他那白胡子的叔伯（倘若美国人能有闲暇活到这般寿数）在整栋宅邸中持续吹奏着芳香的烟斗乐章，全家老小无不随之翩翩起舞；正是在这种对尼古丁最普遍的渗透同化中，孕育出了那个极其反常、却大体令人赞赏的产物——我们的“国人之型”。这个人是幻想家与实干家的奇特复合体：说他是幻想家，是因为其兴奋剂（固然伴有其他成因）使然；说他是实干家，是因为极度活跃的新大陆繁重劳作的必然性迫使他必须如此。他的心智体现在其建筑结构之中——其装潢与闪电般的工期是以牺牲安全、恒久与健康为代价换来的；亦体现在其举止风貌之中——唯有在同人性突兀棱角激烈碰撞的惨痛教训教会他警惕与内敛的必要性之后，他才会表现出谨慎与克制。

他的市镇居所便是雪茄的具象化，恰如不列颠人的居所是大啤酒杯的化身，两者的生存模式皆在极大程度上象征着各自兴奋剂的精魄。

在所有文明国度中，公共建筑工程皆属于这一法则的例外，因为庞大建筑的设计更具世界性，民族特质皆被融入包罗万象的规划之中，而该规划本身便汇聚了世间所有艺术的菁华。

===Original===

With the Turk it is not so; uncatholicized as is his nature, his mosque and his sultan’s seraglio are as definite incarnations of generic peculiarity as his kiosk.
Excusing myself for this digression, I return to the details of the visions which I had commenced.
The night was much darker than it should have been for a hashish-eater’s walk, who, it will be remembered, calls imperatively for light to tinge his visions. The hallucination of the ostrich still remaining, we passed out into the street through the stone gateway at the end of the college terrace. The sky above us was obscured by clouds, but the moon, now at her full, was about three breadths of her disk above the western horizon. I pointed through the trees to her radiant shield, and called Bob’s attention to the peculiar beauty of the view. He clapped his hands in ecstasy, exclaiming, “Behold the eternal kingdom of the moonlight!” From that moment until the planet set, in this kingdom he walked. A silvery deliciousness transfused all things to his sight; his emotions rose and fell like tides with the thrill of the lunar influence. All that in past imaginings he had ever enjoyed of moonlit river views, terraces, castles, and slumberous gardens, was melted into this one vision of rapture.
At length the moon sank out of sight, and a thick darkness enveloped us in the lonely street, only relieved by the corner lamps, which dotted the long and drear prospective. For a while we walked silently. Presently I felt my companion shudder as he leaned upon my arm. “What is the matter, Bob?” I asked. “Oh! I am in unbearable horror,” he replied. “If you can, save me!” “How do you suffer?” “This shower of soot which falls on me from heaven is dreadful!”
I sought to turn the current of his thoughts into another channel, but he had arrived at that place in his experience where suggestion is powerless. His world of the Real could not be changed by any inflow from ours of the Shadowy. I reached the same place in after days, and it was then as impossible for any human being to alter the condition which enwrapped me as it would have been for a brother on earth to stretch out his hands and rescue a brother writhing in the pains of immortality.

===Chinese===

土耳其人则不然；鉴于其天性未曾走向世界主义，他的清真寺与苏丹的后宫，皆同他的凉亭一样，毫无疑问是其族群特性的明确化身。

请容我为这段插叙致歉，现在我重返先前已展开的幻象细节。

那个夜晚对于食大麻者的散步而言显得过于昏暗，读者当会忆起，食大麻者极度需要光亮来为其幻象上色。在鸵鸟的幻觉依然留存之际，我们穿过学院露台末端的石门走上了街道。头顶的天空被阴云遮蔽，但此时的满月正高悬在西方地平线上方约三倍月盘直径的高度。我透过树丛指向她皎洁光辉的盾盘，提醒鲍勃留意这番景致的奇特之美。他欣喜若狂地拍手高呼：“看哪，这月光的永恒王国！”从那一刻起直至明月西沉，他便一直漫步在这座王国之中。在她的注视下，银白色的美妙渗透了万物；他的情感随着月华感召的悸动如潮汐般起伏涨落。过往想象中关于月光沐浴下的江景、露台、城堡与静谧庭园所体会过的一切乐趣，皆消融于这幅狂喜的画卷之中。

终于，月亮沉入地平线之下隐没不见，浓重的黑暗在孤寂的街道上将我们包围，唯有街角的路灯点缀着漫长而凄凉的前路带来一丝光亮。有一阵子，我们默默前行。少顷，依偎着我手臂的同伴剧烈颤抖起来。“怎么了，鲍勃？”我问。“噢！我陷入了无法忍受的恐惧之中，”他回答道，“如果你能救我，快救救我！”“你感觉怎么难受？”“从天上砸落在我身上的这阵煤烟雨太可怕了！”

我试图将他的思绪引向另一道航道，但在他的体验中，他已经走到了暗示无能为力的境地。他那真实的客观世界，绝无法被我们这虚妄暗影世界的任何外力流入所改变。在此后的岁月里我也走到了同样的境地，届时任何凡人想要改变笼罩着我的状态，皆绝无可能，恰如尘世的手足想要伸出援手去拯救在永生痛苦中煎熬翻滚的手足一样徒劳。

===Original===

There are men in Oriental countries who make it their business to attend hashish-eaters during the fantasia, and profess to be able to lead them constantly in pleasant paths of hallucination. If indeed they possess this power, the delirium which they control must be a far more ductile state than any I have witnessed occurring under the influence of hashish at its height. In the present instance I found all suggestion powerless. The inner actuality of the visions and the terror of external darkness both defeated me.
Again, for a short distance, we went without speaking. And now my friend broke forth into a faint, yet bitter cry of “Pray for me! I shall be lost!” Though still knowing that he was in no ultimate danger, I felt that it was vain to tell him so, and, granting his request, ejaculated, “Oh best and wisest God, give peace unto this man!” “Stop! stop!” spoke my friend; “that name is terrible to me; I can not hear it. I am dying; take me instantly to a physician.”
Aware that, though no such physical need existed, there was still a great spiritual one if I would make him calm, I immediately promised him that I would do as he asked, and directed our course to the nearest doctor. Now, demoniac shapes clutched at him from the darkness, cloaked from head to foot in inky palls, yet glaring with fiery eyes from the depths of their cowls. I felt him struggling, and by main force dragged him from their visionary hands. The place wherein he seemed to himself to be walking was a vast arena, encircled by tremendous walls. As from the bottom of a black barathrum, he looked up and saw the stars infinitely removed; they gazed mournfully at him with a human aspect of despairing pity, and he heard them faintly bewailing his perdition. Sulphureous fires rolled in the distance, upbearing on their waves agonized forms and faces of mockery, and demon watch-fires flared up fitfully on the impenetrable battlements around him. He did not speak a word, but I heard him groan with a tone that was full of fearful meaning.
And now, in the midst of the darkness, there suddenly stood a wheel like that of a lottery, surrounded by one luminous spot, which illustrated all its movements.

===Chinese===

在东方各国，有人以在幻境期间照料食大麻者为业，自称能够持续引导他们走在令人惬意的幻觉路径上。倘若他们真有此等本领，他们所掌控的谵妄必定与我所了解的大相径庭。在那谵妄之中，灵魂一旦启航投入自身选定的潮流，就如吞入大漩涡（Maelstrom）之口的巨舰一般，根本不再受舵或桨的操纵。意志与判断的机能不仅无力指引航向，反而要么死亡，要么彻底受制于庞大的主观冲动。
煤烟雨的恐怖与日俱增。我们艰难地前行，他沉沉地倚靠在我身上，迈着仿佛瘫痪的双脚踉跄而行。每迈出一步都异常艰难；我们路过的每一根路灯柱似乎都化作了一个恶魔，其红色的眼眸在幽暗中眨动，嘲弄着行者的痛苦，其身躯张开铁臂横亘在我们的道路上阻挡前行。此时，一座低沉空洞的大钟开始以庄严的节拍鸣响，其震颤撼动了我们脚下的大地，在空气中弥漫着凄婉的回声。我认出那是荷兰教堂敲响午夜的钟声，但对他而言，那却是末日的丧钟，每一记敲击都饱含着可怕的凶兆。

此时，在黑暗的正中央，骤然耸立起一座形如抽奖转轮的大轮盘，被一处光斑所环绕，照亮了它的一举一动。

===Original===

It began slowly to revolve; its rapidity grew frightful, and out of its opening flew symbols which indicated to him, in regular succession, every minutest act of his past life: from his first unfilial disobedience in childhood﻿—the refusal upon a certain day, as far back as infancy, to go to school when it was enjoined upon him, up to the latest deed of impropriety he had committed﻿—all his existence flew before him like lightning in those burning emblems. Things utterly forgotten﻿—things at the time of their first presence considered trivial﻿—acts as small as the cutting of a willow wand, all fled by his sense in arrow-flight; yet he remembered them as real incidents, and recognized their order in his existence.
This phenomenon is one of the most striking exhibitions of the state in which the higher hashish exaltation really exists. It is a partial sundering, for the time, of those ties which unite soul and body. That spirit should ever lose the traces of a single impression is impossible. De Quincey’s comparison of it to the palimpsest manuscripts, while it is one of the most powerful that even that great genius could have conceived, is not at all too much so to express the truth. We pass, in dreamy musing, through a grassy field; a blade of the tender herbage brushes against the foot; its impression hardly comes into consciousness; on earth it is never remembered again. But not even that slight sensation is utterly lost. The pressure of the body dulls the soul to its perception, other external experiences supplant it; but when the time of the final awaking comes, the resurrection of the soul from its charnel in the body, the analytic finger of inevitable light shall search out that old inscription, and to the spiritual eye no deep-graven record of its earthly triumphs shall be clearer.
The benumbing influences of the body protect us here from much of remorse and retrospective pining. Its weight lies heavily upon the inner sense, and deadens it to perception of multitudes of characters which, to be read, require acutest powers of discernment. When the body is removed, the barrier to the Past goes also.
This fact may perhaps be one of the final causes why the body exists at all. Why are we not born directly into the spiritual world, without having to pass through a weary preliminary experience hemmed in by the gross corporeal nature?

===Chinese===

它开始缓缓旋转；速度变得骇人听闻，从它的开口处飞出一个个象征符号，按规则的顺序向他指示出他过往人生中每一个最微小的行为：从他孩提时代第一次违抗父母的不孝——早在襁褓初学之时，某天因被严令上学而悍然拒绝——直至他最近所犯下的一桩失礼之举；在那些燃烧的象征徽记中，他的全部存在宛如闪电般在他眼前飞掠而过。彻底被遗忘的事物——最初发生时被视作微不足道的事物——小如削下一根柳条般的举动，皆如离弦之箭般从他的感知中飞掠而过；然而他却将它们忆作真实发生的事件，并在自己的生存序列中辨认出了它们的先后顺序。

这一现象，是大麻至高亢奋状态真实存在的几个最引人注目的体现之一。这是将灵魂与肉体维系在一起的纽带在彼时的局部割裂。心灵会磨灭任何单一印象的痕迹，是绝无可能的。德·昆西将其比作重写羊皮纸手稿，这即便对于那位伟大天才所能构想出的比喻而言也是最具震撼力的之一，用来表达真理却丝毫不过分。我们在如梦如幻的沉思中漫步穿过一片草地；一叶娇嫩的青草拂过足跟；那印象几乎未曾进入清醒意识之中；在尘世间，它再也不会被忆起。然而，即便这般轻微的感觉也绝未彻底磨灭。肉体的重压钝化了灵魂对它的感知，其他外部体验取代了它；但当最终觉醒的时辰来临、灵魂从肉身的骨灰坛中复活之时，无可避免的光明的剖析之指必将寻获那段古老的铭文，而在属灵之眼的注视下，即便它在尘世凯旋时深镌的勋绩，也绝不会比这更清晰。

肉体麻木的钝化作用，在此尘世庇护着我们免受诸多悔恨与追忆凄苦的煎熬。它的重负沉沉压在内在感知之上，麻痹了它对大量字符的察觉——若想读懂这些字符，需要最敏锐的洞察机能。当肉体被撤去时，阻隔通往往昔的藩篱亦随之烟消云散。

这一事实，或许正是肉体之所以存在的终极原因之一。为何我们未能直接降生在属灵的世界之中，而无须历经被粗鄙肉体本性所困囿的漫长疲惫的预备体验？

===Original===

May not the answer be something like this? Were the soul, at its first creation, introduced directly into the world where truth is an intuition, and stands in the dazzling light of its own essence, the dreadful sublimity of the view might prove its annihilation. We accordingly pass first through an apprenticeship, in which we have nothing colossal either to learn or to do; and eternal verities dawn on us slowly, instead of breaking in like lightning. The Phenomenal is at first all that we know; we have qualities and quantities, and through the period of infancy are content with novel acquisitions in this field. Next, we become aware of certain faculties of induction, investing us with the power of apprehending the Notional, which never comes within the grasp of Sense: we learn relations which exist only to the thought, yet are deemed still as valid experiences as if they were tasted or handled. Last of all, we mount into the Intuitional domain, and, without any of the props of Sense in any way to steady us, either by sensations perceived or suggesting relations, we know universal principles of Being face to face. Up this gradual stairway of Sense, Understanding, Intuition, we mount to that height from which we are able to behold, with some degree of calmness, the infinite fields of intuitive Beauty and Truth, when the screen of the bodily is removed, and the scope of vision belonging to our highest faculty is realized to be immeasurably beyond all that our most rapturous visions ever conceived it. Without this slow indoctrination, the soul might have flamed out in dazzling momentary irradiance, and then been extinguished in eternal nothingness.
If it be true that the bodily is thus our shield from the lethal glories of the purely spiritual world, and also from the full force of painful memories in the past, we can easily see how a most terrible retribution might be wreaked upon the soul by permitting it to stand through eternity without any covering to dim the events of its earthly time. Doubtless the spirit, interiorly in a state harmonious to the celestial concourse, will be invested with a spiritual body﻿—a body which, while it does not press heavily, like ours of the earthy, will still so condition states of mind as to permit no inflow but that of delightful impressions.

===Chinese===

答案难道不可能是这般模样吗？倘若灵魂在初创之时，便被直接引入真理即是直觉、真理挺立于自身本质耀目光芒之中的世界，那一景观骇人的崇高，恐怕便足以令灵魂湮灭。因此，我们首先经历一段学徒期，在其中我们既无庞然大物需要研习，亦无惊天动地之事需要躬行；永恒的真实是徐徐破晓降临于我们心头，而非如闪电般骤然劈入。现象界起初是我们所认知的一切；我们拥有质与量，并在婴儿期满足于在这一领域中斩获的新知。随后，我们察觉到了某些归纳机能，赋予了我们领悟纯概念界的力量——那是感官绝无法触及的领域：我们学会了仅存在于思绪之中的关联，却依然将其视作真切有效的体验，宛如曾被品尝或触摸过一般。最终，我们攀升入直觉的领域，在毫无感官支柱以感知或提示关联来支撑我们的情况下，我们得以与存在的普世法则面对面相对。沿着感官、知性、直觉这一步步递进的阶梯，我们攀登至那座制高点；从那里，当肉身的屏障撤去、当我们认识到属于我们最高机能的视界范围远超最狂喜的幻象所能设想的一切时，我们便能够以某种程度的从容，凝望直觉之美与真理的无垠旷野。若缺少这种循序渐进的熏陶启蒙，灵魂或许会在耀目的瞬间光华中燃尽，随后在永恒的虚无中熄灭。

倘若肉体确实是我们抵御纯灵界致命荣光、以及抵御往昔痛苦回忆全部冲击的护盾，我们便能轻易洞悉：任由灵魂毫无遮掩地伫立于永恒之中、任由尘世时日的诸般过往毫无削减地显露无遗，将会是对灵魂施加的何等可怕的报应。毫无疑问，在内在状态与天国圣会融洽相谐的心灵，必将被披覆上一副属灵的躯体——这副躯体虽不像我们尘世的躯壳那样沉重压抑，却仍能调节心智状态，只允许愉悦印象的涌入。

===Original===

But let the soul to which such societies and such garments are uncongenial, from the evils which he loves, stand bare in the presence of the Nemesis of his past life, with the wondrous light of the New World irradiating the terrors of her countenance, and all the symbols of fire and scorpion-stings will but faintly image the agonies of the view. Well, then, does Paul pray, “Not that I may be unclothed, but clothed upon.”
I left the narration of my story while we were still walking toward the doctor’s. At length, reaching there, we found him still sitting in his office, although it was now eleven o’clock.
I tried in vain to obtain the first word with him; for Bob, who seemed, according to the frequent nature of the hashish hallucination, suspicious of some wrong about to be done him, would not allow me to say anything which might tinge the opinion of the physician. He persisted in affirming that he was at the point of death, although denying that he felt pain in any place which he could touch. He was totally unable to inform the doctor of the cause of his condition; but I at last managed to tell him myself. Like the great majority of practitioners, he knew nothing of the nature of the drug, and could only shake his head and presage evil from observing the singular phenomena which characterized his patient’s outward conduct. He told Bob that he was very foolish to have made the experiment; was in imminent danger﻿—might die; would give him a powder﻿—ahem!
With such preeminent consolation he was poulticing the poor fellow’s excited mind, when I took advantage of his going out for a dose of ipecac to follow him unostentatiously, feigning the intention of helping him to prepare the dose. The moment that we were in another room, I said, with as much vehemence as was possible without Bob’s overhearing it, “For Heaven’s sake, if you have any mercy, tell that man that he is in no danger! He *is* in none whatever. I have made the same experiment repeatedly, and I assure you that all he wants is the calm assurance of his safety.”
My earnest manner satisfied him of my truth, and he accordingly went into the room where Bob was still sitting, and comforted him very much in the same manner that my doctor used with me when I was terrified in my first experiment.

===Chinese===

然而，凡因眷恋罪恶而与这等团契及这等法衣格格不入的灵魂，赤身裸体伫立在其往昔人生的复仇女神（Nemesis）面前，任凭新世界的奇妙光芒映照出她面庞的无尽恐怖，世间关于烈火与蝎螫的一切象征，相比之下都不过是对那幅景象痛苦的微弱描摹而已。难怪保罗（Paul）祈祷道：“并非愿意脱下衣服，乃是愿意穿上衣服。”

在我暂停叙述时，我们正朝医生的住处走去。终于抵达那里时，尽管当时已是夜里十一点，我们发现他仍端坐在诊室里。

我徒劳地试图抢先同医生交谈几句；因为鲍勃出于大麻幻觉屡见不鲜的多疑秉性，似乎怀疑有人要害他，不肯容我说出任何可能影响医生判断的话。他一口咬定自己命在旦夕，却否认自己身上任何能摸得到的地方感到疼痛。他完全无法告知医生导致其病症的成因；但我终于设法亲自告知了医生。正如绝大多数行医者一样，他对这种药物的本质一无所知，在观察到患者外在举止表现出的离奇现象后，只能连连摇头，预言凶兆。他告诉鲍勃：做这个实验愚蠢透顶；性命危在旦夕——随时可能丧命；得给他开一包药粉——咳咳！

正当他用这般高明的慰藉敷裹可怜朋友亢奋的心神之际，我趁他外出取一剂吐根（ipecac）之机，假装打算协助他调配药剂，不动声色地跟随其后。刚一踏入隔壁房间，我便在不让鲍勃听见的前提下，竭尽全力厉声说道：“看在上帝的份上，如果您有一丝怜悯，就告诉那个人他毫无危险！他*绝无*任何危险。我曾反复做过同样的实验，我向您保证，他所需要的唯独是对其安危的镇定保证！”

我恳切的态度让他信服了我的真诚，于是他走回鲍勃依然端坐的房间，以非常类似于我的医生在我初次实验受惊吓时安慰我的方式抚慰了他。

===Original===

He told him, laughingly, to be in no apprehension whatever for results, as he would certainly recover from his present feelings intact.
In an instant Bob became perfectly calm, and the former state of happiness succeeded his agonies. We passed out of the doctor’s office and began returning home. On the way he supposed himself a Mandarin freshly come from some triumph over invading tribes. Like myself, in the vision of my victorious march, he heard anthems of laud and glory pealing in his ears; but he did still more﻿—he played one of the instruments himself. Always a man of fine musical imagination, and quite a brilliant pianist, he now possessed a power of melodious creation unknown in his highest natural states. Setting his lips so as to send forth sounds in imitation of a bugle, he played in my hearing a strain of his own impromptu composition so beautiful that it would have done credit to any player upon wind instruments that ever obtained celebrity. For a quarter of a mile I enjoyed this unexpected rapture of music, in the utmost astonishment at a phenomenon I had never conceived of before.
We reached home. The experimenter lay down, and through all the night he was wrapped in visions of the utmost ecstasy. I sat beside his bed for hours, and always became aware of the moment of his highest exaltation by some strain after the manner of that which had cheered our way up the dark and lonely hill, bursting from his lips, even in sleep, with delicious melody. In the morning he awoke at the usual time; but, his temperament being perhaps more sensitive than mine, the hashish delight, without its hallucination, continued for several days. Bob never took it again.
The next case which I shall mention is that of my friend Fred W﻿—﻿—, who, although now having abandoned hashish forever, still, from the first experiment he made with it, was so delighted with the spell that for several months he made trial of its powers, as successfully, if never to the same extent, as myself. His temperament was the sanguine and nervous commingled; his taste for the arts amounted to a passion. The initiatory test of hashish which he made gave him only its space and time expanding effects, but he had obtained a sufficient glimpse of its weirdness to make him try again.

===Chinese===

他笑着告诉他，对后果完全无须挂虑，他当前的感受必定会完好无损地彻底消退复原。

顷刻间，鲍勃完全镇定了下来，先前的幸福状态取代了他的痛苦。我们步出医生的诊室，踏上了归途。在路上，他误以为自己是一位刚刚战胜外侮部落凯旋的大官（Mandarin）。正如我在胜利行军的幻象中一样，他听见赞美与荣耀的赞歌在耳畔鸣响；但他做得更甚——他亲自吹奏起其中一种乐器。他向来富于精妙的音乐想象力，且是位颇为出色的钢琴手，此刻他竟拥有了一种在自然常态的巅峰亦未曾具备的旋律创作力。他收紧双唇摹仿军号的声音，在我耳畔奏响了一段他自己即兴创作的乐调，其旋律之优美，足以让任何名垂青史的管乐大师亦引以为荣。在四分之一英里（a quarter of a mile）的行程中，我尽情陶醉在这份始料未及的乐音狂喜之中，对我此前从未设想过的奇迹惊叹不已。

我们回到了家中。实验者躺下歇息，并在整个夜晚皆被极致狂喜的幻象所包裹。我在他的床边守候了数小时；每逢他达到亢奋的最高峰时，他总会以类似于照亮我们在黑暗孤寂山岗上归途的那种调子，即便在沉睡中亦从唇齿间迸发出美妙动人的旋律，让我清晰知晓。次日清晨他在寻常时分醒来；然而，或许由于他的体质比我更为敏感，大麻带来的愉悦感在脱离了幻觉之后，依然持续了数日之久。鲍勃从此再未服食过大麻。

我接下来要提及的案例，是我的一位朋友弗雷德·W（Fred W——）；尽管他如今已永远戒绝了大麻，但在初次尝试之后，他便深深为这道魔咒所倾倒，以至于接连数月都在测试其效力，其成功程度纵使不及我这般深入，却也同样成效斐然。他的体质是多血质与神经质的交织体；他对艺术的品味堪称炽烈的狂热。初次尝试大麻仅赋予了他空间与时间膨胀的效应，但他已足够窥见其诡异之处，促使他再次尝试。

===Original===

Upon many a bright moonlight night have I walked with him through streets made balmy by the breath of the summer night, or rowed our boat along the silvery river while he lay in ecstatic musing upon the stern seat. In the dreams of such a man as he or the last one whom I mentioned, by sympathy I lived almost as delicious a hashish life as in my own. Once do I remember well, while we were floating noiselessly between those twin welkins, the glorious sky above us, and its image mirrored in the stream below, his beholding in the clouds that lifted their beamy masses on the western horizon a resplendent city, built amphitheatrically like Algiers, yet in every dome and architrave beautiful with the taintless lustre of marble. And now he cried, “Sing! my mood is congenial to the ethereal spirit of music.” I softly hummed “*Spargi d’amaro pianto.*” “That is ecstasy!” he broke forth once more. “Do you remember those words, ‘Architecture is frozen music?’ With your ascending notes I saw grand battlements rise immensely into the sky; with the descending tones they sank again, and through all your song I have sat enamored of one delicious dance of Parian marble.”
