But his most wonderful experience﻿—wonderful for its exceeding beauty, but still more so for the glimpse which it gave him of the mind’s power of sympathetic perception, was a vision which he had after that which I have just related. Having taken hashish and felt its influence already for several hours, he still retained enough of conscious self-control to visit the room of a certain excellent pianist without exciting the suspicion of the latter. Fred threw himself upon a sofa immediately on entering, and asked the artist to play him some piece of music, without naming any one in particular.
The prelude began. With its first harmonious rise and fall the dreamer was lifted into the choir of a grand cathedral. Thenceforward it was heard no longer as exterior, but I shall proceed to tell how it was internally embodied in one of the most wonderful imaginative representations that it has ever been my lot to know.
The windows of nave and transept were emblazoned, in the most gorgeous coloring, with incidents culled from saintly lives.

===Chinese===

不知有多少个皎洁的月夜，我曾陪他漫步在夏夜呼吸所润泽的芬芳街道上，或是当他在船尾座上沉浸于狂喜的玄思时，在银白色的江面上荡起双桨。在像他或我上一位提及的朋友的梦境中，借由共情共鸣，我活出了一段同我自己亲历一样美妙的大麻生涯。有一次我记忆犹新：当我们无声地漂浮在双重苍穹之间——头顶是壮丽的天宇，身下江流中倒映着天宇的幻影——他望着西方地平线上耸立起光彩夺目云团之中的一座璀璨之城，那城形如阿尔及尔（Algiers）般宛若露天剧场般层叠而建，然而每一座穹顶与额枋皆散发着大理石无瑕的光泽，美轮美奂。此刻他高呼道：“唱吧！我的心境与音乐空灵的精魂相契相合。”我轻声哼唱起《洒下辛酸的泪滴》（*Spargi d’amaro pianto*）。“那便是极乐！”他再次情不自禁地喊道，“你记得‘建筑是凝固的音乐’那句话吗？随着你上升的音符，我看见宏伟的城堞巍峨耸入云霄；随着下降的音调，它们又重新隐落；在你整首歌曲中，我都沉醉于帕罗斯（Parian）大理石绝美的狂舞之中。”

然而他最奇妙的体验——不仅因其极致的美妙，更因它让他窥见了心智共情感知力的惊人奥秘——乃是紧随我方才记述的经历之后的一幅幻象。在服下大麻并感受到药效已有数小时之后，他依然保留着足够的清醒自控力，前往拜访一位卓越钢琴手的房间，而未引起后者的丝毫怀疑。进门之后，弗雷德立即倒在沙发上，请那位艺术家为他演奏一首乐曲，并未指明具体的曲目。

序曲奏响了。随着最初和谐的起伏跌宕，造梦者被提升至一座宏伟主教座堂的唱诗班中。自此往后，乐音不再被视作身外之物，而我将进而讲述它如何内在化为我有幸领教过的最奇妙的想象显现之一。

中殿与耳堂的彩绘花窗以极绚烂的色彩饰满了从圣徒生平中采撷的诸般事迹。

===Original===

Far off in the chancel, monks were loading the air with essences that streamed from their golden censers; on the pavement, of inimitable mosaic, kneeled a host of reverent worshippers in silent prayer.
Suddenly, behind him, the great organ began a plaintive minor like the murmur of some bard relieving his heart in threnody. This minor was joined by a gentle treble voice among the choir in which he stood. The low wail rose and fell as with the expression of wholly human emotion. One by one the remaining singers joined in it, and now he heard, thrilling to the very roof of the cathedral, a wondrous miserere. But the pathetic delight of hearing was soon supplanted by, or rather mingled with, a new sight in the body of the pile below him. At the farther end of the nave a great door slowly swung open and a bier entered, supported by solemn bearers. Upon it lay a coffin covered by a heavy pall, which, being removed as the bier was set down in the chancel, discovered the face of the sleeper. It was the dead Mendelssohn!
The last cadence of the death-chant died away; the bearers, with heavy tread, carried the coffin through an iron door to its place in the vault; one by one the crowd passed out of the cathedral, and at last, in the choir, the dreamer stood alone. He turned himself also to depart, and, awakened to complete consciousness, beheld the pianist just resting from the keys. “What piece have you been playing?” asked Fred. The musician replied it was “Mendelssohn’s Funeral March!”
This piece, Fred solemnly assured me, he had never heard before. The phenomenon thus appears inexplicable by any hypothesis which would regard it as mere coincidence. Whether this vision was suggested by an unconscious recognition of Mendelssohn’s style in the piece performed, or, by the awakening of some unknown intuitional faculty, it was produced as an original creation, I know not, but certainly it is as remarkable an instance of sympathetic clairvoyance as I ever knew.
Dan, the partner of my hashish-walk mentioned as occurring in the town of P﻿—﻿—, was, at the same time as myself, a member of the college. The Coryphaeus of witty circles, and the light of all our festivals, he was still imaginative in higher spheres, and as worthily held the rostrum and the bard’s chair as his place by the genial fireside or generous table.

===Chinese===

在圣坛深处，修道士们令空气中充盈着从黄金香炉中涌出的香氛；在无可比拟的马赛克铺石地面上，跪倒着大批虔诚的信众，默然祈祷。

突然，在他身后，宏伟的管风琴奏起了一段如泣如诉的小调，宛若某位吟游诗人在哀歌中宣泄心曲的低语。在他伫立的唱诗班中，加入了一声温柔的高音，与这小调相和。那低沉的哀鸣起伏跌宕，洋溢着全然纯粹的人类情感。其余的歌者逐一加入其中；此刻，他听见一曲奇妙的《怜悯曲》（miserere）震颤着升腾至主教座堂的顶棚。然而，听觉的哀婉欣喜很快便被他下方建筑主体中呈现的新景象所取代，抑或不如说与之交融在一起。在中殿的最远端，一扇大门缓缓敞开，一具灵柩台在庄严抬棺人的抬举下缓缓迈入。台上摆放着一具覆盖着厚重棺罩的灵柩；当灵柩台安放在圣坛前并揭开棺罩时，沉睡者的面容展现了出来。那竟是死去的门德尔松（Mendelssohn）！

悼歌的最后一个休止符渐次消逝；抬棺人迈着沉重的脚步，将棺木抬过一扇铁门，安放在地下墓穴之中；人群逐一步出主教座堂，最终，在唱诗班席位上，唯独剩下造梦者独立伫立。他也转身离去，随后在完全苏醒的清醒意识中，看到那位钢琴手刚刚将手从琴键上移开歇息。“你方才弹奏的是什么曲子？”弗雷德问。琴师答道，那是“门德尔松的《葬礼进行曲》（Funeral March）！”

弗雷德向我郑重保证，他平生此前从未听闻过这首曲目。因此，将该现象视作单纯巧合的任何假说，皆无法对此作出合理解释。究竟这幅幻象是由于他在潜意识中辨识出了所弹曲目中的门德尔松风格，还是借由某种未知的直觉机能的觉醒而产生的独创性造物，我不得而知；但这毫无疑问是我所知晓的最卓越的共情透视（sympathetic clairvoyance）范例之一。

前文提及在P市曾与我同行漫步大麻之途的同伴丹，彼时亦与我同在该学院就读。作为妙语连珠圈子里的领军魁首（Coryphaeus）以及我们所有节庆聚会的灵魂人物，他在更崇高的领域依然富于想象力，无论是在讲坛与吟游诗人的座椅上，还是在温馨的壁炉旁或丰盛的餐桌边，皆同样当之无愧。

===Original===

A poet, and an enthusiastic lover as well as performer of music, I supposed that the effect of hashish upon his susceptible temperament would be delightful in the extreme. But to such a result, the time at which he took the drug was one of the most unfavorable in the world﻿—when his nervous system was in a state of even morbid excitability. We had started together on a walk when the thrill came on. And such a thrill﻿—or, rather, such a succession of thrills﻿—it is wonderful how a human organism could sustain. At first a cloud of impenetrable mystery enwrapped him; then upon the crown of his head a weight began to press. It increased in gravity without gaining bulk, and at last, breaking through the barrier of the skull, it slid down the spinal column like lightning, convulsing every nerve with one simultaneous shudder of agony.
This sensation was repeated again and again, until, with horror, he called on me to return, as assured as I had ever been in my first experiments that death was soon to be the result of the shock. I instantly obeyed his wish, and on reaching his room he lay down. Of a sudden all space expanded marvelously, and into the broad area where he reclined marched a multitude of bands from all directions, discoursing music upon all sorts of instruments, and each band playing a different march on a different key, yet all, by some scientific arrangement, preserving perfect harmony with each other, and most exquisitely keeping time. As the symphony increased in volume, so also did it heighten in pitch, until at last the needle-points of sound seemed to concentre in a demon music-box of incredible upper register, which whirled the apex of its scream through the dome of his head, inside of which it was playing.
Now, on the wall of the room, removed to a great distance by the hashish expansion, a monstrous head was spiked up, which commenced a succession of grimaces of the most startling yet ludicrous character. First its ferociously bearded under jaw extended forward indefinitely, and then, the jaw shooting back, the mouth opened from ear to ear. Now the nose spun out into absurd enormity, and now the eyes winked with the rapidity of lightning.
Yet suffering in Dan bore an excessive over-ratio to mirth.

===Chinese===

作为一位诗人，以及音乐的狂热爱好者与演奏者，我本以为大麻作用于他那易感的体质，必将产生极致愉悦的效果。然而欲求这般结果，他服药的时机却堪称世间最不利的时刻之一——当时他的神经系统正处于近乎病态的极度亢奋状态之中。当我们结伴开始散步时，战栗袭来了。那是怎样的一阵战栗啊——或者更确切地说，怎样的一连串战栗啊——人类机体竟然能够承受得住，实在令人匪夷所思。起初，一团浓得化不开的神秘阴云将他笼罩；接着，一股重压开始落在他的颅顶之上。其重量与日俱增，体积却未曾膨胀，最终冲破颅骨的阻隔，如闪电般顺着脊柱滑落而下，让每一根神经在同时爆发的剧痛战栗中剧烈抽搐。

这种感觉一次又一次地反复重演，直到他在惊恐之中呼唤我折返；正如我在最初实验中所深信的那样，他笃信死亡很快便会作为这场震颤的收场而降临。我立即顺从了他的愿望，一到他的房间他便躺了下来。突然之间，整个空间神奇地膨胀开来，在他躺卧的宽敞区域内，无数支军乐队从四面八方阔步而入，操着各式各样的乐器演奏乐章，每支乐队皆在不同的调性上奏响不同的进行曲，然而通过某种精妙科学的编排，彼此之间却保持着完美的和谐，并在节拍的把控上极尽精巧。随着交响乐音量的增强，音高亦随之攀升，直至最后，那些针尖般的乐音似乎汇聚在一个拥有不可思议极高音域的恶魔八音盒中，在它正演奏着的他的头颅穹顶内部，将其尖叫的顶点旋转穿透。

此时，在被大麻膨胀拉至极遥远处的房间墙壁上，钉着一颗巨大的头颅，开始做出一连串既惊悚万分又滑稽绝伦的鬼脸。起初，它那长满凶恶胡须的下颌无休止地向前伸展，随后下颌猛地缩回，大嘴直咧到耳根。时而，鼻尖拉长成荒诞的庞然大物；时而，双眼以闪电般的速度频频眨动。

然而，在丹身上，痛苦所占的比例远远压倒了欢乐。

===Original===

In his greatest pain he had framed a withering curse against someone who had entered the room, but when he tried to give it utterance his lips failed in their office as if paralyzed. I gave him water when his thirst had become extreme, and the same sensations of a cataract plunging down his throat which I have before described occurred so powerfully that he set the glass down, unwilling to risk the consequences of his draught.
Returning to consciousness, he did not, however, recover from the more moderate hashish effects for months. The nervous thrills which I have related reappeared to him at intervals, and his dreams constantly wore a hashish tint. Indeed, in all cases which I have known, this drug has retained a more enduring influence than any stimulant in the whole catalogue.
A number of experiments made upon other persons with more or less success, yet none of them characterized by any phenomenon differing from those already detailed, prove conclusively that upon persons of the highest nervous and sanguine temperaments hashish has the strongest effect; on those of the bilious occasionally almost as powerful a one; while lymphatic constitutions are scarcely influenced at all except in some physical manner, such as vertigo, nausea, coma, or muscular rigidity. Yet to this statement there are striking exceptions, arising out of the operation of some latent forces of vitality which we have not yet included in our physical or psychical science. Until the laws which govern these are fully apprehended, hashish must ever remain a mystery, and its operation in any specific case an uncertainty.

===Chinese===

在最剧烈的痛苦中，他曾在心头酝酿了一通恶毒的咒骂对准某个走进房间的人，但当他试图将其吐露时，双唇却如同瘫痪般失去了机能。在他极度焦渴时我递给他水喝，而我先前描述过的那种宛若瀑布倾泻入喉的感觉如此强烈地席卷了他，以至于他放下了水杯，不愿冒着痛饮的后果去冒险。

在重返清醒之后，他在数月内皆未曾从较为温和的大麻后遗效应中彻底复原。我所记述的神经战栗不时在他身上重现，他的梦境亦始终染着大麻的色彩。诚然，在我所知晓的一切案例中，这种药物所留下的深远影响，比整部药典中的任何兴奋剂都要恒久持久。

在其他人身上进行的一系列实验或多或少取得了成功，但没有一个案例呈现出与已详述的现象相异的特征；这些实验无可辩驳地证明：在极度神经质与多血质体质的人身上，大麻的效力最为强劲；在胆汁质体质的人身上，偶尔亦能产生近乎同等强度的效果；而淋巴质体质的人除眩晕、恶心、昏迷或肌肉僵直等某些肉体机能表现外，几乎根本不受影响。然而，对于这一论断亦存在引人注目的例外，源于某些我们尚未纳入心身物理科学范畴的潜在生命机能运作。在支配这些机能的法则被完全理解之前，大麻必将永远是一个不解之谜，而其在任何具体案例中的作用机制，亦必然是一桩未知之数。

## IX The Shadow of Bacchus, the Shadow of Thanatos, and the Shadow of Shame / 第九章 巴克科斯之影、塔纳托斯之影与羞耻之影
===Original===

Once more at the table I was seized by the hand of the hashish genie. Dinner was nearly over, and I escaped into the street without being suspected.
Street did I say? Ah no! That conventional synonym of all dust, heat, and garbage is unheard upon the sunny slopes of Mount Bermius, where I wandered Bacchus-smitten among the Maenads. Through the viny shades that embowered our dance of rapture, Haliacmon threw the gleam of his sky-bright waters, and the noon rays, sifted through leaves and clusters, fell on us softened like gold into the lap of Danae. Grapes above us, grapes around us, grapes everywhere, made the air fragrant as a censer. They dropped with the burden of their own sweetness; they shed volatile dews of ecstasy on every sense. Constellations of empurpled orbs, they dissolved the outer light of heaven by their own translucency; and from their hemispheres of silver down, which looked toward the sun, to those hemispheres which turned in upon our dance a gaze half of jet, half of sapphire, they transmitted the gentle radiance, until it bathed our cheeks and foreheads in the hue of autumn sunsets. Together with troops of Bacchantes I leaped madly among the clusters; I twirled my thyrsus, and cried *Evoë Bacche* with the loudest. On a delicious wind of fragrance the fawn-skin floated backward from my shoulders, and the viny leaves and tendrils of my garland caressed my temples lovingly. I drank the blood of grapes like nectar; I sang hymns to the son of Semele; I reeled under the possession of the divine afflatus. Around me in endless mazes circled beauteous shapes of men and women; with hands enclasped we danced and sang, and the Maenad houris overshadowed me with their luxuriant and disheveled hair.
Now, wandering from their throng in a rapture which, too high to be imparted, sought some solitude where it could shed itself forth unheard, I passed through the college gateway, and began traveling up the long walk which finally led into the woods toward the east﻿—finally, I say, for I remember even now the measureless stretch of the journey.
At length, reaching the borders of the stream which had before come to me the Nile, and which, through my whole hashish life, witnessed many a delirium of joy and torture, I sat down upon a high, precipitous bank which overhung the water, and gave myself up to my fantasia.

===Chinese===

在餐桌旁，我再度被大麻精灵的巨手捕获。晚餐已近尾声，我未引人怀疑便脱身逃到了街上。

我说是街？啊，不！在贝尔米乌斯山（Mount Bermius）阳光普照的斜坡上，根本听闻不到那个代表着一切尘土、酷热与污物的世俗同义词——在那里，我中了巴克科斯之毒，在狂女迈纳德斯（Maenads）群中漫游。透过荫蔽着我们狂喜之舞的葡萄藤浓荫，哈里亚克蒙河（Haliacmon）投射出其如晴空般澄澈明亮的水光，而穿透枝叶与果穗筛落的正午光芒，轻柔地洒落于我们身上，宛若当年倾入达那厄（Danae）怀抱中的黄金。头顶是葡萄，四周是葡萄，目之所及皆是葡萄，令空气芬芳馥郁得如同一只香炉。它们因自身的甜美沉甸甸地下垂，向每一种感官散发着令人狂喜的挥发性甘露。那些由紫晶球体汇聚而成的星座，借由自身的晶莹剔透消融了外界的天光；从朝向太阳的那一面银绒半球，到向我们的舞蹈投来半如墨玉、半如蓝宝凝视的另一面半球，它们传递着柔和的辉芒，直至将我们的双颊与额头都浸染在秋日落日的霞彩之中。我与成群的巴克科斯侍从一同在果簇间狂乱飞跃；我旋转着手中的酒神杖，用最高亢的声音呼喊着“埃沃埃，巴克科斯（Evoë Bacche）！”在阵阵甜美的馥郁芬芳之风中，小鹿皮从我的肩头向后飘扬，我花环上的藤叶与卷须爱抚着我的太阳穴。我如饮琼浆般痛饮葡萄之血；我向塞墨勒（Semele）之子高唱颂歌；在神圣灵感的附体之下步履踉跄。在我身旁，无数身形优美的男女在无休止的迷宫中盘旋环绕；我们执手相挽，起舞高歌，迈纳德斯仙子以其丰盈蓬乱的长发将我笼罩。

如今，怀着一种因过于崇高而无法与人分享的狂喜，我脱离了他们的人群，去寻觅某处僻静之所，以便能悄然倾泻胸臆；我穿过学院的大门，踏上了通往东面树林的漫长小径——我说“漫长”，是因为时至今日我仍记得那段旅程无边无际的漫长。

终于，我抵达了那条小溪的岸边——这条溪流此前曾化作我眼中的尼罗河，并在我整个大麻生涯中见证过无数次欢乐与折磨的谵妄——我坐在一处俯瞰水面的高耸陡峭的土岸上，彻底沉溺于我的奇想之中。

===Original===

The stream broadened and grew glorified: it was the Amazon, and on a towering bluff I was gazing down the liquid sweep toward the sea. Now a great ship came gliding past, lifting its topgallant far above my post of observation, and men ran up the shrouds to peer curiously at me. With her long pennant flying and every inch of her courses shaken out, she passed me majestically, and I climbed down to the brink of the river to catch the last look at her, and see it returned from another inquisitive gazer at the taffrail.
I wandered completely through the woods, and came out into a broad field upon the farther side. Before me rose the buildings of a grand square, in some city whose name, whose nation I could not even imagine, so utterly foreign did it appear to anything in the world of modern days. In the centre of the square a mighty host had assembled to inaugurate the equestrian statue of a hero, which, exquisitely carved in a rose-tinted marble, rose on its colossal pedestal far above their heads. I was drawn toward them by an irresistible impulse, for sculpture and architecture had reached, in that city, the highest ideal of art. I thought of the hero, and seemed to share the glory of his triumph.
Then out of the borders of the dense wood from which I had just emerged came a hot and hissing whisper, “Kill thyself! kill thyself!” Shuddering, I turned to see who spoke. No one was visible. Again, with still intenser earnestness, the whisper was repeated; and now unseen tongues syllabled it on all sides and in the air above me. To these words soon arguments were added, until the atmosphere seemed all aglow with fierce breathings of “Thou shalt be immortal; thou shalt behold the hidden things of God. The Most High commands thee to kill thyself.” “My God!” I cried, “can this be true? I will obey thee, and drink in the eternities.”
Feeling myself as mightily pressed on to do the deed as by a direct behest of Deity; daring not, for my soul’s sake, to resist the utterances; and immeasurably exalted with the prelibation of the glories that, in a moment, were to flow in upon me, in frantic fury I drew forth my knife, opened it, and placed it at my throat. Another heartthrob, and all would have been over.

===Chinese===

溪流变得开阔且辉煌无比：它成了亚马逊河，我站在高耸的绝壁上，俯瞰着奔流向海的浩荡水势。这时，一艘巨舰轻盈滑过，高耸的顶桅帆远远掠过我观察哨位的前方，水手们纷纷爬上缆绳，好奇地窥探着我。船上长长的三角旗迎风飘扬，底帆寸寸张满，威严地从我身旁驶过；我攀下河岸，想要再多看她最后一眼，并迎上了船尾栏杆处另一位探头张望者的目光。

我完全穿过了树林，来到了另一侧广袤的原野。在我眼前耸立着一座宏伟广场的建筑群，那是某座名字与所属国度我都无法想象的城市，它与现代世界的一切显得如此格格不入。在广场中央，浩浩荡荡的人群聚集在一起，为一位英雄的骑马雕像举行落成典礼——那座用玫瑰色大理石精雕细刻的雕像，高高耸立在巨大的基座上，远远凌驾于众人的头顶。一种不可抗拒的冲动将我引向他们，因为在这座城市里，雕塑与建筑已达到了艺术的至高典范。我思索着这位英雄，仿佛也分享着他胜利的荣光。

随即，从我方才走出的茂密树林深处，传来了一阵滚烫而咝咝作响的低语：“自尽吧！自尽吧！”我战栗着转过身，想看清是谁在说话。目之所及空无一人。那低语再次响起，语气更加强烈而急迫；紧接着，无形的舌头在四面八方以及我上空的空气中一字一顿地吐露这声音。很快，言语中又添了各种理由，直到整片空气仿佛都因狂热的吐息而炽热燃烧：“你将获得永生；你将目睹上帝隐藏的奥秘。至高者命令你自尽。”“我的上帝啊！”我呼喊道，“这难道是真的吗？我愿顺服你，饮尽永恒。”

我感到自己受到一股强烈的驱使去行此举，宛若领受了神明的直接旨意；为了我的灵魂，我不敢违抗这言辞；又因预尝了顷刻间便要涌向我的无上荣耀而无比亢奋，在一阵狂乱的冲动中，我拔出了小刀，将其打开，架在了自己的喉咙上。只要心跳再搏动一次，一切便将终结。

===Original===

It was just then that I felt the blow of some invisible hand strike my arm; my hand flew back, and, with the force of the shock, the knife went spinning away into the bushes. The whispers ceased. I looked up into heaven, and lo! from zenith to horizon, an awful angel of midnight blackness floated, with poised wings, on the sky. His face looked unutterable terrors into me, and his dreadful hand, half clenched, was hollowed above my head, as if waiting to take me by the hair. Across the firmament a chariot came like lightning; its wheels were rainbow-suns that rolled in tremendous music; no charioteer was there, but in his place flashed the glory of an intense brightness. At its approach the sable angel turned and rushed downward into the horizon, that seemed to smoke as he slid through it; and, thank God! from Azrael I was saved.
How many a temptation, which the ordinary grossness of the ear prevents us from ascribing to its true external source, and which we would fain persuade ourselves is nothing but our own thought, would come to us thus in a real demon-voice were the bands of the body but a little loosened! In how many attractions toward good and repulsions from evil would we then feel the touch of angel hands! The world at present is, to a great degree, Sadducee; it scoffs at the Spiritual, which for blindness it can not discern, and lives in meat and bone. The best men conservatively go halfway to shake hands with the most unspiritual skeptic, and acknowledge with him that the most reasonable way to account for our wooings and our warnings is the reaction of soul upon itself. What these poor lovers of the earthy will do when they arrive among the realities of another world, it is hard to say. When this poor, mouldy, moth-eaten, time-tattered cloak of the corporeal, which for years has flapped about their heads in the gusts of worldly fortune, or tangled in its wet rags the feet of the soul that were trying to climb higher﻿—when this poor cloak falls off, and they stand transformed into that most dreaded bugbear of their previous lives, spirit﻿—we may, perhaps, hear them cry out in agony, “Oh, my beloved garment! my best suit! what will become of thee?” and see them diving headlong off the battlements of light to recover the only part of their human wardrobe in which they can feel comfortable.

===Chinese===

正是在那一刻，我感到一只无形之手重重击打在我的手臂上；我的手猛然缩回，伴随着撞击的力道，小刀旋转着飞落进了灌木丛深处。耳语戛然而止。我仰望苍穹，看哪！从天顶至地平线，一位漆黑如子夜的可怖天使正舒展着双翼悬停在天幕之上。他的面容向我投射来无可言喻的恐惧，他那半握的可怕巨手在我头顶上方虚拢成爪，仿佛正等待着揪住我的头发。一辆战车如闪电般横贯苍穹；其车轮乃是虹彩之日，在惊天动地的仙乐中轰鸣滚转；车上并无御者，但在御者之位上闪耀着炽烈耀眼的光芒。随着战车临近，黑衣天使转身向地平线俯冲而下，当他滑落穿过时，地平线仿佛泛起了青烟；感谢上帝！我从死亡天使亚兹拉尔（Azrael）手中获救了。

有多少诱惑，只因肉耳寻常的迟钝而未能被我们归结为真正的外在源头，我们还妄图自欺欺人地认为那不过是自己脑海中的念头——倘若肉体的羁绊能稍微松绑片刻，这些诱惑便会以真实魔鬼的嗓音向我们降临！届时在多少向善的吸引与对恶的排斥中，我们都会真切感受到天使之手的抚触！当今世界在极大程度上都是撒都该人（Sadducees）；它嘲弄着因盲目而无法洞察的属灵之境，只苟活在骨肉皮囊之中。最善良的人也保守地走了一半的路，与最不具灵性的怀疑论者握手言和，并与后者一同承认：解释我们所受的招引与警示最合理的方式，乃是灵魂对自身的反作用。这群可怜的凡尘恋慕者，有朝一日抵达另一个世界的现实之中时究竟会如何自处，着实难以断言。当这具肉体那破旧、发霉、遭虫蛀、被岁月撕扯得破烂不堪的斗篷——多年来它在世俗际遇的狂风中围绕着他们的头颅拍打，或以潮湿的破布纠缠着试图向上攀登的灵魂之足——当这具可怜的斗篷脱落，他们蜕变成为前世最畏惧的幽灵之态时，我们或许会听见他们痛苦地哀号：“啊，我心爱的衣裳！我最好的套装！你该怎么办啊？”并看着他们从光明的城垛上头朝下纵身跃下，只为寻回他们人类衣橱中唯一能让他们感到舒适的部分。

===Original===

After my escape from death I returned to the border of the brook, and began pacing back and forth upon a long flat stone around which the shelve of the bank curved. My surroundings instantly became theatrical; the woods behind me changed into a back scene, and on a grand stage I was holding entranced a great audience, whom I beheld before me rising in colossal tiers from earth to sky. The part I was acting was that of a victorious soldier in some tragedy whose words I improvised, and, growing rapidly into the interest of my speech, I poured forth words﻿—now in prose and now in verse﻿—which swayed the hearers like a whirlwind. As my manner increased in earnestness, I saw a strange and dreadful look of suspicion overshadowing every face of the thousands in my audience. From the searching stare of the pit I sought relief in turning my face toward the boxes. The same stony glance from under eyebrows met me still, and when I raised my despairing countenance to the galleries, the same quenchless scrutiny poured down upon me. “Can it be?” I asked myself. “Oh! they know my secret!” and at that instant one maddening chorus broke from the whole theatre: “Hashish! hashish! he has eaten hashish!” Then, with one tumultuous uprising, the concourse fled. From the stage I crept away, consumed by an unutterable shame. I sought a place upon the bank of the stream still lower down, where a large hazel-bush leaned over the water, and beneath its branches I crouched. The helmet and corslet were gone. I looked at my garments, and beheld them foul and ragged as a beggar’s. From head to foot I was an incarnation of the genius of squalidity.
Alas! even here I could not hide. I had chosen my asylum on the very pavement of a great city’s principal thoroughfare. Children went by to school, and pointed at me in derision; loungers stood still, and searched me with inquisitive scorn. The multitude of man and beast all eyed me; the very stones of the street mocked me with a human raillery as I cowered against a side wall in my bemired rags.
Now, mixing with the throng of passersby, and no more real than they, two of my college friends came strolling along the brook. They saw and knew me, and my shame reached its unbearable height when I saw them approach me with looks which I thought also of sarcasm.

===Chinese===

死里逃生之后，我折返至溪水边，开始在一块长条平石上来回踱步，溪岸的斜坡正环绕着这块石头。我周遭的一切瞬间化作了戏剧舞台；我身后的树林变成了舞台布景，在一座宏伟的戏台上，我让台下庞大的观众群体听得如痴如醉，只见他们如巍峨的阶梯般从大地层层耸立直插云霄。我扮演的是某部悲剧中一位凯旋的将士，台词皆由我即兴发挥，随着我对演讲的兴致迅速高涨，我倾泻而出的言辞——时而散文，时而韵律——如旋风般左右着听众的心绪。随着我的举止越发激昂，我看到观众席中数千张面孔的每一张上，都笼罩着一层诡异而可怕的疑云。为了躲避正厅后座那审视的凝视，我将面孔转向包厢寻求宽慰。然而迎面而来的依然是眉毛下冷酷如石的目光；而当我将绝望的面容抬向楼座时，同样的逼视如火般倾泻而下。“这可能吗？”我自问，“天哪！他们知道了我的秘密！”就在那一刻，整座剧场爆发出一阵令人发狂的合唱：“大麻！大麻！他吃了大麻！”随后，伴随着一阵骚乱的耸动，整个人群四散溃逃。我怀着无可名状的羞耻，从舞台上爬了出去。我在更下游的溪岸上觅得一处所在，那里有一丛巨大的榛树俯垂在水面上，我便蜷缩在它的枝桠之下。头盔与胸甲皆已不见。我看了看自己的衣着，发现它们肮脏破烂得宛如乞丐。从头到脚，我俨然成了邋遢穷酸之神的化身。

哀哉！即便在这里我也无处遁形。我所选的避难所竟是一座大都市主要干道的人行道。孩子们成群结队去上学，指着我嘲笑讥讽；闲汉们驻足不前，以探究的轻蔑目光打量着我。成群结队的人与牲畜皆注视着我；当我在满身污泥的破衣烂衫中瑟缩在一堵侧墙旁时，连街上的石块都在以人类的尖酸言辞对我极尽嘲弄。

这时，两位大学好友混杂在过往的人群中漫步沿着小溪走来，其真实程度并不亚于那些路人。他们看见并认出了我，而当我看到他们带着自以为同样充满讥讽的神情向我走来时，我的羞耻感达到了难以承受的顶峰。

===Original===

But, as they drew nearer, they spoke to me kindly, and asked what was the matter with me, and why I sat hiding behind the hazel-branches. I hesitated for a moment, but, on their promise of secrecy, told them my latest experience. They sat down beside me, and in the diversion of talking the hallucination passed by.
Suddenly an unconquerable apprehension possessed me. There were certain secrets which for my right arm I would not have betrayed, and yet I felt imperatively called upon to speak them. I struggled against the impulse with the thews of a spiritual Titan. I was determined to conquer it, yet, that I might provide against a failure, I conceived this expedient. Picking up a withered leaf from the bank of the stream, I called the two to hold it, each by a portion of the rim, while I grasped it by the stem. In this way we raised the leaf toward heaven, and with our other hands clasped in each other, I solemnly repeated this adjuration: “As this leaf shall be withered in the fiery breath of the final day, so may we be withered in the vengeance of the Eternal if aught that may be said here pass our lips without the consent of us all three.” Here we all said “Amen,” and once more I was at ease. I did not betray my own secrecy.
When I became calm the two left me and returned to their rooms. I wandered back to my old station on the high ledge, where I had seen the ship sweep by me, and sat down. When I looked into the sky between the treetops, the sun seemed reeling from his place, and the clouds danced around him like a chorus. I turned my eyes downward, and found that I was surrounded by warriors, who had come to bear me an invitation to the coronation of Charlemagne. “In a moment I will go with you,” was my reply, “but first I must drink; I am dreadfully athirst.” The stream was rattling away directly below me; my distance from it by the most easy roundabout descent was not more than fifty feet, yet I must relate, even at the risk of saying too much of the hashish expansion of distance, that in going to it I seemed passing down the league-long ridge of a mountain. I walked, I roamed, I traveled before reaching it, and at last, lying down upon the water’s brim. I drank such streams of refreshment as appeared to lower the flood.

===Chinese===

然而，随着他们走近，他们却温和地同我说话，问我究竟怎么了，为何要躲在榛树枝后。我犹豫了片刻，但见他们许诺保密，便将我方才的遭遇告知了他们。他们坐在我身旁，在交谈的调剂下，幻象逐渐消散。

突然间，一种无可遏制的恐惧攫住了我。有些秘密就算砍掉我的右手我也绝不会出卖，然而我却感到一股无可抗拒的驱使，逼迫我必须将其说出。我凭借属灵巨人的筋骨力量同这股冲动搏斗。我决意征服它，但为了防备万一失败，我想出了一个权宜之计。我从溪岸边拾起一片枯叶，叫他们两人各执叶缘的一部分，我自己则捏住叶柄。就这样，我们将树叶举向天空，另一只手紧紧相握，我庄严地重复了这道誓言：“若在此所言之语未经我们三人一致同意便传出双唇，愿我们如同此叶在末日烈焰的气息中枯萎一般，在永恒之主的复仇中化为灰烬。”念罢，我们众人都说了“阿门”，我这才重新安下心来。我并未泄露自己的秘密。

待我恢复平静后，他们两人便离开我返回了寝室。我漫步折返至那处高耸岩壁上的旧哨位，在那里我曾见巨舰从我身旁滑过，随后坐了下来。当我透过树梢凝望天空时，太阳仿佛在原地蹒跚摇晃，云彩如合唱队般围绕着他翩翩起舞。我俯瞰下方，发现自己被一众勇士团团包围，他们是来向我传递出席查理曼大帝（Charlemagne）加冕典礼的请柬。“我片刻后便随你们去，”我回答道，“但我得先喝口水；我渴得厉害。”溪水正在我正下方汩汩奔流；即便走最缓和的迂回下坡路，我与它的距离也不过五十英尺，但我必须直言——哪怕冒着过多描述大麻对距离膨胀效应之嫌——在走向溪水的过程中，我仿佛正顺着一条长达数里（league）的山脊往下走。在抵达溪边之前，我走啊走，漫游穿梭，长途跋涉，最终躺倒在水畔。我痛饮了那般清泉甘洌，以至于水流仿佛都被我喝得退落了下去。

===Original===

On my return, after toiling up the weary steep, my escort had gone, and I certainly could not blame them, if the length of my unceremonious absence seemed to them half as great as it did to me.
Wandering through jungle, heather, brake, and fern﻿—through savanna, oak-opening, and prairie﻿—through all imagined and unimaginable countries﻿—now despairing of my ability ever to find my way, and now plucking heart to press on﻿—through many a day, or rather through one boundless perpetual day of journeying, I went until I reached home.
Throwing myself down upon a bed, I was immediately compensated for all past sufferings. In the middle of a vast unpeopled plain I stood alone. With one quick ravishment I was borne upward, as on superhuman wings, until, standing on the very cope of heaven, I looked down and saw beneath me all the worlds that God has made, not wheeling upon their beamy paths through ether, nor yet standing without significance like orbed clods.
By an instantaneous revealing I became aware of a mighty harp which lay athwart the celestial hemisphere, and filled the whole sweep of vision before me. The lambent flame of myriad stars was burning in the azure spaces between its strings, and glorious suns gemmed with unimaginable lustre all its colossal framework. While I stood overwhelmed by the vision, a voice spoke clearly from the depth of the surrounding ether: “Behold the harp of the universe.”
In an instant I realized the typification of the grand harmony of God’s infinite creation, for every influence, from that which nerves the wing of Ithuriel down to the humblest force of growth, had there its beautiful and peculiar representative string.
As yet the music slept, when the voice spake to me again, “Stretch forth thine hand and wake the harmonies.” Trembling, yet daring, I swept the harp, and straightway all heaven thrilled with an unutterable music. My arm strangely lengthened, I grew bolder, and my hand took a wider range. The symphony grew more intense; overpowered, I ceased, and heard tremendous echoes coming back from the infinitudes. Again I smote the chords, but, unable to endure the sublimity of the sound, I sank into an ecstatic trance, and was thus borne off unconsciously to the portals of some new vision.

===Chinese===

在费力攀爬上令人疲惫的陡坡返回之后，我的护卫队已经离开了，如果在我看来那段失礼的离开时间在他们眼中显得有一半那么漫长，我自然丝毫不能怪罪他们。

穿越丛林、石楠、灌木与蕨类——穿过稀树草原、橡树疏林与大草原——穿过所有想得到与想不到的国度——时而为自己能否找到回路而绝望，时而鼓起勇气继续前行——历经数日之久，或者更确切地说，穿过一段漫无边际、永无休止的长途跋涉之日，我终于回到了家中。

我一头倒在床上，过去的全部苦痛立刻得到了补偿。我孑然一身伫立在一片辽阔无人的平原中央。伴随着一阵倏忽而至的狂喜恍惚，我宛如生出超人之翼般被托举向上，直至伫立在天穹的最顶端；我俯瞰下方，见上帝所创造的所有世界皆展现在我脚下，它们既非在以太中耀眼的轨道上旋转，亦非如无意义的圆形泥块般静止不动。

借由瞬间的显现，我觉察到有一架巨大的竖琴横亘于天半球之上，充盈了我眼前的全部视野。无数繁星柔和闪烁的火焰在琴弦间的蔚蓝虚空中燃烧，璀璨的太阳镶嵌其上，以难以想象的光华点缀着它那庞大的骨架。正当我被眼前的景象震撼得不知所措时，周围以太的深处传来了一声清晰的话音：“看哪，宇宙的竖琴。”

刹那间，我领悟到了上帝无限造物之宏大和谐的象征具象，因为从鼓舞天使伊图里尔（Ithuriel）羽翼的力量，下至生长中最卑微的动力，世间万般的每一种造化，在此处都拥有其优美而独特的代表之弦。

此时音乐尚在沉睡，那声音再次对我说道：“伸出你的手，唤醒这些和谐之音吧。”我颤抖着，却又带着决绝的大胆，轻轻拨动了竖琴，整个天堂顿时因无可名状的乐音而战栗悸动。我的手臂奇异地伸长，我的胆量愈发壮大，我的双手划出更宽广的音域。交响乐越发激昂澎湃；被其气势压倒的我停下了手，听闻惊天动地的回响自无垠深处荡漾而归。我再次抚弄琴弦，然而因无法承受那音律的至高崇高，我陷入了狂喜的迷离恍惚之中，就这样在不知不觉中被带往了某种崭新异象的门廊前。

## X *Nimium*﻿—The *Amreeta* Cup of Unveiling / 第十章 过量：揭示之甘露杯
===Original===

It was shortly after the last vision which has been related that I first experienced those sufferings which are generated by a dose of hashish taken to prolong the effects of a preceding one.
Through half a day I had lain quietly under the influence of the weed, possessed by no hallucination, yet delighted with a flow of pleasant images, which passed by under my closed eyelids. Unimaginable houris intoxicated the sense with airy ballet-dances of a divine gracefulness, rose-wreathed upon a state of roses, and flooded with the blush of a rosy atmosphere. Through grand avenues of overarching elms I floated down toward the glimpse of an impurpled sky, caught through the vista, or came glancing through the air over gateways of syenite, rose-tinted by the atmosphere, and in Egypt walked among the Caryatides. Up mystic pathways, on a mountain of evergreens, the priests of some nameless religion flocked, mitre-crowned, and passed into the temple of the sun over the threshold of the horizon. Now, “ringed with the azure world,” I stood, a lonely hemisphere above me, a calm and voiceless sea beneath me; suddenly an island of feathery palms floated into the centre of the watery expanse, and gauze-winged sprites dropped down upon its shore. Now landscapes of strange loveliness slowly slid before me, but stopped at my will, that I might wander far up their music-haunted bays, and sit, bathed in sunlight, on the giant rock-fragments which lay around their unpeopled shores.
But once did I open my eyes and leap up in fear; for into the gardens of the Grecian villa where I walked among statues and fountains, an incongruous horde of Indian braves burst whooping, in their war-dance; and writhing in savage postures, with brandished club and tomahawk, they called upon my name, and looked for me through the olive-trees. Lying down again, I soared into the dome of St. Peter’s, and, lighting on the pen of the apostle, laid my hand upon the angel’s shoulder. A mighty stretch of arm indeed; yet, to the hashish-eater, all things are possible.
About the hour of noon I found the effects of my first dose rapidly passing off. It had been a small one, possibly fifteen grains, and, as I have said, produced no hallucination; yet so enamored had I become of the procession of pleasing images which it set in motion, that, for the sake of prolonging it, I took five grains more.

===Chinese===

正是在前述最后一次异象过后不久，我初次体会到了因服用追加剂量以延长前次药效而引发的剧痛折磨。

大半天的时间里，我都静静躺倒在大麻的影响之下，未受任何幻觉的侵扰，只陶醉在一连串令人愉悦的意象洪流之中，它们自我的闭拢的双眸下缓缓掠过。难以想象的胡里仙女（houris）以具有神圣优雅姿态的空灵芭蕾之舞沉醉着我的感官，在玫瑰铺就的高台上环佩玫瑰，沐浴在泛着红晕的粉色苍穹之中。穿过由拱形榆树掩映的宏伟林荫道，我向下漂浮，迎着自林荫深处隐约投射来的紫霞天光；或在半空中轻掠过那些被气氛染成玫瑰色的闪长岩（syenite）门廊，并在埃及漫步于女像柱（Caryatides）之间。沿着常青之山上的神秘幽径，某不知名教派的祭司们头戴主教冠冕，成群结队蜂拥而至，越过地平线的门槛步入太阳的神庙。此刻，“环抱于蔚蓝的世界”，我孑然伫立，头顶是一方寂寥的天半球，脚下是平静无声的汪洋；突然，一座长满羽状棕榈的小岛漂移至这片浩渺水域的中央，生着薄纱翅膀的精灵飘然降落在它的岸边。这时，一幅幅有着奇特美景的风光在我眼前缓缓滑过，却能随我的心意驻留，以便我能漫步深入那些回荡着仙乐的海湾，在无人的岸边错落散布的巨岩碎片上，沐浴着阳光安然而坐。

然而唯有一次，我猛然睁开双眼惊恐地跃起；因为在我穿行于雕像与喷泉之间的希腊庄园花园里，竟冲进了一群格格不入的印第安勇士，呼啸着跳起了他们的战舞；他们扭动着野蛮的体态，挥舞着战棍与手斧，高呼着我的名字，在橄榄树丛中搜寻我的踪迹。当我再次躺下时，我翱翔登上了圣伯多禄大教堂（St. Peter’s）的穹顶，落在了使徒的手笔之上，并将手搭在天使的肩头。这手臂的延展确实惊人；然而对于食大麻者而言，世间万事皆有可能。

大约正午时分，我发现初次剂量的药效正在迅速退去。那本是一个小剂量，大概只有十五格令（grain，约一克），正如我所言，并未产生幻觉；但我已深深迷恋上它所唤起的那一幕幕令人愉悦的意象盛宴，为了延长这种体验，我便又服下了五格令。

===Original===

Hour after hour went by; I returned to the natural state, and gave up all idea of any result from the last dose. At nine o’clock in the evening I was sitting among my friends writing, while they talked around me. I became aware that it was gradually growing easier for me to express myself; my pen glanced presently like lightning in the effort to keep neck and neck with my ideas. At first I simply wondered at the phenomenon, without in the least suspecting the hashish which I had eaten nine hours before. At last, thought ran with such terrific speed that I could no longer write at all. Throwing down my pen, I paced the room, chafed my forehead, and strove to recover quiet by joining in the conversation of those about me.
In vain! intense fever boiled in my blood, and every heartbeat was the stroke of a colossal engine. Within me I felt that prophecy of dire suffering which the hashish-eater recognizes as unmistakably as were it graven by the finger of light, but whose signs, to all but him, are incommunicable. In agony of spirit I groaned inwardly, “My God, help me!”
The room grew unbearable with a penetrating glory of light. I mounted into it, I expanded through it, with a blind and speechless pain, which, in my very heart’s core, was slowly developing itself into something afterward to burst forth into demoniac torture. I felt myself weeping, and ran to a looking-glass to observe the appearance of my eyes. They were pouring forth streams of blood! And now a sudden hemorrhage took place within me; my heart had dissolved, and from my lips the blood was breaking also.
Still, with that self-retent which a hashish eater acquires by many a bitter discipline, I withheld from my friends the knowledge of my torture, averting my face until the hallucination passed by. Indeed, as often occurs at such times, a paralysis of speech had taken place, which prevented me from communicating with others: not physical, but spiritual; for the recital of such pain seems to increase it tenfold by drawing its outlines more distinctly to the perception, and therefore I did not dare to give it utterance.
And now a new fact flashed before me. This agony was not new; I had felt it ages ago, in the same room, among the same people, and hearing the same conversation. To most men, such a sensation has happened at some time, but it is seldom more than vague and momentary.

===Chinese===

一个钟头又一个钟头过去了；我恢复了常态，对最后一剂药效的发挥已不再抱任何希望。晚上九点钟，我正坐在朋友们中间伏案疾书，他们则在我身旁谈笑。我发觉自己表达思绪正变得越来越得心应手；不多时，我的笔锋便如闪电般飞驰，极力与奔涌的思绪并驾齐驱。起初我只是对这一现象感到惊异，丝毫未曾怀疑到九个钟头前服下的那剂大麻。最终，思绪以一种恐怖的极速狂飙，以至于我根本无法再动笔书写。我扔下钢笔，在房间里来回踱步，揉搓着额头，试图通过加入身边众人的交谈来重获平静。

徒劳无功！滚烫的高热在我的血液中沸腾，每一次心跳都宛如庞大机器的重击。在我的内心深处，我感到了那种食大麻者如辨识光明之指所铭刻般确凿无疑、而除他之外对所有人皆不可言传的剧烈苦痛预兆。在精神的极大痛苦中，我暗自呻吟：“我的上帝，救救我！”

整个房间充盈着穿透一切的光芒荣耀，令人难以忍受。我升腾入其中，在其中向外膨胀，伴随着一种盲目而无言的剧痛——这痛楚正潜伏在我的心核深处，缓慢演变，随后便要爆发成为魔鬼般的酷刑折磨。我感到自己在抽泣，便奔向一面镜子去查看自己的双眼。只见双眼竟在向外倾泻着一道道血流！紧接着，我的体内发生了突发的体内大出血；我的心脏已经消融，鲜血同样也正从我的双唇间喷涌而出。

然而，凭借食大麻者通过无数次严苛磨砺所习得的自我克制力，我向朋友们隐瞒了我受折磨的真相，将脸转过去，直到幻觉平息。事实上，正如每逢此类时刻常常发生的那样，我陷入了一种失语瘫痪状态，使我无法与旁人交流：这并非肉体上的瘫痪，而是属灵的；因为叙述这等痛苦，只会因在感知中将痛楚的轮廓勾勒得更为清晰而使其剧增十倍，因而我不敢将其付诸言辞。

此时，一个全新的事实在我眼前闪现。这种剧痛并非初次经历；早在几个纪元以前，在同一间屋子里，在同样的一群人中间，听着同样的一番交谈，我就曾体验过这等剧痛。对大多数人而言，这类感觉都曾在某个时刻发生过，但往往只是模糊而稍纵即逝的。

===Original===

With me it was sufficiently definite and lasting to be examined and located as an actual memory. I saw it in an instant, preceded and followed by the successions of a distinctly-recalled past life.
What is the philosophy of this fact? If we find no grounds for believing that we have ever lived self-consciously in any other state, and can not thus explain it, may not this be the solution of the enigma? At the moment of the soul’s reception of a new impression, she first accepts it as a thing entirely of the sense; she tells us how large it is, and of what quality. To this definition of its boundaries and likenesses succeeds, at times of high activity, an intuition of the fact that the sensation shall be perceived again in the future unveiling that is to throw open all the past. Prophetically she notes it down upon the indestructible leaves of her diary, assured that it is to come out in the future revelation. Yet we who, from the tendency of our thought, reject all claims to any knowledge of the future, can only acknowledge perceptions as of the present or the past, and accordingly refer the dual realization to some period gone by. We perceive the correspondence of two sensations, but, by an instantaneous process, give the second one a wrong position in the succession of experiences. The soul is regarded as the historian when she is in reality the sibyl; but the misconception takes place in such a microscopic portion of time that detection is impossible. In the hashish expansion of seconds into minutes, or even according to a much mightier ratio, there is an opportunity thoroughly to scrutinize the hitherto evanescent phenomena, and the truth comes out. How many more such prophecies as these may have been rejected through the gross habit of the body we may never know until spirit vindicates her claim in a court where she must have audience.
At length the torture of my delirium became so great that I could no longer exist unsustained by sympathy. To Bob, as possessing, from his own experience, a better appreciation of that which I suffered, I repaired in preference to all others. “Let us walk,” said I; “it is impossible for me to remain here.”
Arm-in-arm we passed down the front steps. And now all traces of the surrounding world passed away from before me like marks wiped from a slate.

===Chinese===

而在我身上，它却足够清晰而持久，足以作为一段真实的记忆受到审视与定位。我刹那间看清了它，其前后承接着一段被清晰忆起的前尘过往。

这一事实背后的哲理究竟为何？倘若我们找不到理由相信自己曾在任何其他状态下具备自我意识地生活过，因而无法以此做出解释，那么这是否就是解开谜团的钥匙？在灵魂接纳某种新印象的瞬间，她起初只是将其完全当作感官之物来接纳；她告知我们它有多大，具备何种特质。在心智高度活跃的时刻，紧随对其界限与相似性的这番界定之后的，乃是一种直觉——直觉到在未来揭开一切过往的大揭示中，这一感觉将再度被感知。她充满预言性地将其记录在日记那不可磨灭的纸页上，确信它将在未来的启示中显现。然而我们这些因思维惯性而拒绝承认对未来拥有任何预知主张的人，却只能认可源自当下或过去的感知，因而便将这双重领悟归结到了某个消逝的往昔。我们感知到了两种感觉之间的契合对应，却借由一种瞬间的思维机制，在经验的序列中给第二种感觉安排了一个错误的位置。灵魂明明是女先知（sibyl），却被当成了史官；但这一误解发生在如此微观的刹那之间，以至于根本无法被察觉。在大麻将数秒膨胀为数分钟、甚至依据更为庞大的倍率进行延展的过程中，便有契机彻底审视这些此前转瞬即逝的现象，而真相亦由此水落石出。在这具肉体粗鄙的习性之下，究竟还有多少这般预言曾被弃置不顾，我们或许唯有等到灵魂在必定受审的法庭上申辩自身的主张时，方能真正知晓。

最终，我谵妄中的折磨变得如此剧烈，以至于若无同情的抚慰我将无法继续支撑下去。由于鲍勃（Bob）根据切身体验能更好地体谅我所承受的苦楚，我便抛开所有人唯独走向了他。“我们去走走吧，”我说，“我不可能继续呆在这里了。”

我们手挽着手走下门前的台阶。此时此刻，周遭世界的所有痕迹宛如石板上被擦除的字迹一般，从我的眼前彻底荡然无存。

===Original===

When we first emerged from the building, I noticed that the night was dark, but this was the last I knew of anything external. I was beyond all troubles from earth or sky; my agonies were in the spiritual, and there all was terrific light. By the flame of my previous vision the corporeal had been entirely burned off from about the soul, and I trod its charred ruins under foot without a remembrance that they had ever been sensitive or part of me. A voice spoke to me, “By the dissolution of fire hast thou been freed, to behold all things as they are, to gaze on realities, to know principles, to understand tendencies of being.”
I now perceived that I was to pass through some awful revelation. It proved to be both Heaven and Hell, the only two states in the universe which together comprehend all free-agent creatures, whether in the Here or the hereafter. Of both I drank tremendous draughts, holding the cup to my lips as may never be done again until the draught of one of them is final.
Over many a mountain range, over plains and rivers, I heard wafted the cry of my household, who wept for me with as distinct a lamentation as if they were close at hand. Above all the rest, a sister mourned bitterly for a brother who was about to descend into hell!
Far in the distance rolled the serpentine fires of an infinite furnace; yet did this not seem to be the place to which I was tending, but only the symbol of a certain spiritual state which in this life has no representative. And now the principles of being, which the prophetic voice had foretold that I should see, suddenly disclosed themselves. Oh, awful sight! Iron, for they were unrelenting; straight as the ideal of a right line, for they were unalterable; like colossal railways they stretched from the centre whereon I stood. Yet more were they to me than their mere material names, for they embodied an infinity of sublime truth. What that truth was I strove to express to my companion, yet in vain, for human language was yet void of signs which might characterize it. “Oh God!” I cried, “grant me the gift of a supernatural speech, that I may, if ever I return, come to humanity like a new apostle, and tell them of realities which are the essence of their being!” I perceived that this, also, was impossible.

===Chinese===

当我们初次从楼宇中走出时，我注意到夜色沉沉，但这却是我对外界事物最后的知觉。我已经超脱于来自大地或天空的一切纷扰；我的痛楚全都发生于灵性之中，而在那里，一切皆是令人惊骇的可怖光辉。借由我先前异象中的烈焰，肉体已从灵魂的周遭彻底焚毁殆尽，我将烧焦的残骸踩在脚下，丝毫记不起它们曾有过知觉或是曾经属于我的一部分。一个声音对我说道：“借由烈火的销蚀你已获自由，得以按万物的本相目睹一切，得以凝视现实，得以知晓真理之原则，得以领悟存在之倾向。”

我此刻意识到，自己即将穿过某种可怖的启示。事实证明，那同时包含着天堂与地狱——这是宇宙中共同涵盖了一切具有自由意志之受造物的仅有两种状态，无论是在今生还是来世。我对二者皆痛饮下了巨量之汁，将杯盏举至唇边，其决绝程度唯有当饮尽二者之一作为终局时方能复现。

越过千山万壑，越过平原与河流，我听闻随风飘来了我家人的呼喊，他们为我痛哭哀号，其悲戚的号哭声清晰得宛若近在咫尺。在所有人之中，一位妹妹正为即将坠入地狱的兄长痛彻心扉地哀悼！

在极目远方，一座无垠熔炉的蜿蜒烈火正在翻滚；然而这似乎并非我所奔赴的终点，而仅仅是某种在此生中找不到对应表象的精神状态的象征。此时，那位预言之声曾预告我将得见的“存在之法则”，赫然显现。啊，那是何等令人战栗的景象！坚如玄铁，因其冷酷无情；笔直如理想中的直线，因其不可更改；宛若巍峨无际的铁轨，从我伫立的中心向前延伸。然而对我的意义而言，它们远远超越了其单纯的物质之名，因为它们具象化了无尽的崇高真理。那真理究竟为何，我极力试图向我的同伴表达，然而一切皆是徒劳，因为人类的语言尚且缺乏能够描摹它的符号。“啊，上帝！”我呼喊道，“赐予我超自然言辞的天赋吧，以便倘若我得以生还，我能宛若一位新使徒般重返人间，向世人宣讲构成其生命本质的真理！”然而我意识到，这同样绝无可能。

===Original===

But vaguely, then, like some farsighted one who points his brethren through the rack and tempest to a distant shore, should I ever be able to disclose what I had seen of the Real to men who dwelt amid the Shadowy.
For days afterward I remembered the unveiling. I myself knew that which it disclosed, yet could not tell it; and now all the significance of it has faded from my mind, leaving behind but the bare shard and husk of the symbols.
The railway which I saw appeared twofold: one arm led toward the far fires of my torture, the other into a cloudy distance which veiled its end completely from my sight. Upon the first I traveled, yet not on wheels, for I felt my feet still upbearing me through all the stages of an infinitely rapid progress.
Symbols﻿—symbols everywhere. All along my journey they flashed forth the apocalypse of utterly unimagined truths. All strange things in mind, which had before been my perplexity, were explained﻿—all vexed questions solved. The springs of suffering and of joy, the action of the human will, memory, every complex fact of being, stood forth before me in a clarity of revealing which would have been the sublimity of happiness but for their relation to man’s tendencies toward evil. I was aware at the time (and I am no less so now) that, to a mind in its natural state, the symbols by which I was taught would be marrowless and unmeaning; yet so powerfully were they correspondences to unpreconceived spiritual verities, that I can not refrain from giving one or two of them in this recital.
Hanging in a sky of spotless azure, within the walls of my own heart, appeared my soul as a coin flaming with glories, which radiated from the impress of God’s face stamped upon it. This told me an unutterable truth of my being. Again the soul appeared as a vast store of the same coin shed prodigally upon the earth. Through clefts in the rocky wall which rose beside my way were thrust, in a manner expressive of wondrous craft, barbed talons, which, grasping the coin one by one, as a fishhook holds the prey, drew them slowly in, while I stood helpless, shrieking in the desert loneliness. As each piece of my treasure slid through the crevices, I heard it fall, with a cruel metallic ring, upon the bottom of some invisible strongbox, and this ring was echoed by a peal of hollow laughter from within.

===Chinese===

那么，唯有隐约地——宛若某位远见卓识之人，穿过风暴与残云向他的弟兄们遥指远方的彼岸那般——我有朝一日才可能向栖身于暗影之中的人们，揭示我所窥见的“真实”。

此后的数日里，我都清楚记得那场揭示。我自己通晓它所显露的一切，却无法言传；而如今，关于它的一切深刻含义已从我心中褪色，只留下了那些象征纯粹的碎片与空壳。

我所见到的铁轨呈现为两条分岔：一条通往我承受酷刑的远方烈火，另一条则伸入一片将其终点彻底遮蔽在我视线之外的云雾苍茫之中。我顺着第一条道路跋涉，却并非乘坐车轮，因为在急速飞掠的各个阶段中，我感到自己的双脚始终支撑着我前行。

象征——目之所及皆是象征。在我的整段旅途之中，它们不断闪耀迸发出关于全然不可想象之真理的启示。心灵中所有此前令我困惑不解的奇特事物，皆得到了阐明——所有悬而未决的棘手难题皆迎刃而解。痛苦与欢乐的源泉、人类意志的运作、记忆、存在的每一处错综复杂的事实，皆以一种启示的澄澈清晰展现在我眼前；若非它们同人类趋向邪恶的倾向息息相关，这本该是至高无上的至福。我当时便清醒地意识到（如今亦深信不疑）：对于处于常态的心智而言，那些启迪我的象征必将被视作毫无血肉与意义的空谈；然而，它们与那些前所未设想的属灵真理之间的契合呼应是如此强有力，以至于在这一叙述中，我忍不住要列举其中的一两个例子。

高悬在万里无云的蔚蓝天空之中，在我自身的心壁之内，我的灵魂化作一枚闪烁着万道荣耀光芒的钱币，这些光辉皆自其上印刻的上帝容颜中辐射而出。这向我揭示了我存在之无可言喻的真理。紧接着，这灵魂又显现为大量同一款式的金币，被肆意挥霍散落在大地之上。透过耸立在我道路两旁的岩壁石缝，伸出了一只只带有倒钩的利爪，其动作流露出惊人的诡谲狡诈；它们像鱼钩挂住猎物那般将金币一枚枚攫住，缓缓拖入缝隙之中，而我只能无助地伫立着，在荒漠般的孤寂中绝望惨叫。随着我宝藏的每一枚碎片滑过石缝，我听见它带着冰冷残酷的金属碰撞声跌落入某个看不见的保险箱底部，而这撞击声随即引来了箱内传来的一阵空洞阴冷的哄笑回响。

===Original===

Another truth, though not the most evident one which now suggests itself, but far more dreadful, was taught me by this symbol.
Again, my heart was a deep well of volatile blood, and into it buckets perpetually descended to be drawn up filled, and carried away by viewless hands to nurture the flames which writhed in the distant furnace. Through all this time I was witnessing one more tremendous truth. But one of the representatives still retains its full significancy to my mind, and is communicable also to others.
Standing upon a mountain peak appeared a serene old prophet, whose face was radiant with a divine majesty. In his look, his form, his manner, was embodied all that glorifies the sage; wonderfully did he typify the ideal of the bard﻿—

> “His open eyes desire the truth;

The wisdom of a thousand years

Is in them.”

===Chinese===

借由这一象征，我领悟到了另一重真理——虽非眼下最显而易见的解释，却要可怕得多。

此时，我的心脏化作了一口蓄满翻腾易挥发之鲜血的深井，水桶源源不断地沉入其中，汲满后被无形之手提走，送去喂养远方熔炉中肆虐翻滚的烈焰。在整段时光中，我始终目睹着又一个极其震撼的真理。然而在所有的具象象征中，唯有一处至今仍在我心中保留着完整的意义，且亦能传达给他人。

一位神情祥和的老先知赫然伫立在山巅之上，他的面庞放射出神圣威严的荣光。在他的目光、身形与举止之中，具象化了令圣哲流芳百世的一切品质；他无比绝妙地象征着行吟诗人的理想之境——

> “他洞开的双眸渴慕着真理；

千年的智慧

凝聚其中。”

===Original===

All that science, art, and spotless purity of life can do to ennoble humanity, had ennobled him, and I well-nigh knelt down before him in an ecstasy of worship. A voice spoke to me from the infinitudes, “Behold man’s soul in primeval grandeur; as it was while yet he talked with God.”
Hurried away through immensity, I came, somewhere in the universe, upon a low knoll, flaunting in a growth of coarse and gaudy flowers. Halfway down its slope sat a hideous dwarf, deformed in body, but still much more terrible in the soul, which ogled me through his leaden eyes, or broke in ripples of idiotic laughter over his lax and expressionless lip. One by one he aimlessly plucked the flowers among which he was sitting; he pressed them to his bosom, and leered upon them, as a maniac miser looks upon his treasures, and then, tearing to pieces their garish petals, tossed them into the air, and laughed wildly to see them whirling downward to strew his lap. In horror I averted my face, but a strange fascination drew it back to him again, when once more the terrible voice sounded over my shoulder, “Behold thine own soul!” In an agony I cried, “Why, oh why?” Sternly, yet without a thrill of passion, the voice replied, “Thou hast perverted thy gifts, thou hast squandered thine opportunities, thou hast spurned thy warnings, and, blind to great things, thou playest with baubles. Therefore, behold thyself thus!”
In speechless shame I hid my face and turned away. Now, as with the descent of a torrent, all my violations of the principles which I saw revealed fell down upon my head from the heights of the Past. It was no bewailing over the inexpediency of any deed or thought which I then uttered; from the abysses of my soul a cry of torture went up for discords which I had caused in the grand harmony of universal law. The importance to mere temporal well-being of this act or that, made no difference in the inconceivable pain which I felt at its clear remembrance. Whether, in the Past, I was confronted with a deliberate falsehood or a fictitious addition, for the sake of symmetry, to an otherwise true recital, the horror was the same.

===Chinese===

凡科学、艺术与洁白无瑕的纯洁生活所能赋予人性之崇高，皆已赋予了他，在一阵崇拜的狂喜中，我几乎要向他下跪叩拜。无垠苍穹的深处传来一个声音对我说道：“看哪，那便是人类灵魂在太初之时的壮美；正宛如他尚能同上帝促膝长谈时的模样。”

随即我被裹挟着穿过茫茫虚空，来到了宇宙中的某处，驻足于一座被粗鄙艳俗的花丛所簇拥的低矮土丘之上。在山坡的半腰处坐着一个丑陋畸形的侏儒，他的身躯已然残疾，但其灵魂却更加骇人听闻；那灵魂正透过他铅灰色的眼珠轻佻地斜睨着我，或者在他那松弛木然的嘴角泛起阵阵痴呆的傻笑。他漫无目的地将身旁的一朵朵鲜花连根拔起；将它们紧贴在胸口，带着贪婪狡黠的狞笑端详着它们，宛若疯癫的守财奴审视着自己的财宝；随后，他将那些俗艳的花瓣撕得粉碎，抛向空中，狂乱地大笑着看它们旋转飘落、洒满自己的膝头。在恐惧中我把脸扭向一旁，然而一种诡异的魔力又将我的视线强行拽回到他身上，就在这时，那可怕的声音再次在我肩头响起：“看哪，这便是你自己的灵魂！”在剧痛中我哭喊道：“为何？天哪，究竟是为何？”严厉而毫无一丝波澜的情感，那声音回答道：“你糟蹋了自己的天赋，你挥霍了自己的良机，你蔑视给你的警示，对崇高之物视而不见，却沉溺于微末琐碎的玩物。因此，好生看看你自己的这副模样吧！”

在无言的羞愧中，我捂住脸转身逃避。此时，宛若暴洪倾泻而下，所有我眼见昭示的真理法则因我的违背，从“过去”的高峰重重砸向我的头顶。我此时所发出的呼号，绝非为任何行为或念头的不合时宜而哀叹；而是从我灵魂的无底深渊之中，为自己曾在普世法则的宏大和谐中所制造的种种不和谐音，发出的撕心裂肺的酷刑哀号。这桩或那桩行径对于世俗福祉的无关紧要，在清晰忆起它所引发的难以想象的剧痛面前，根本毫无分别。无论在“过去”我所面对的是一桩刻意的谎言，还是为了追求通顺对称而在原本真实叙述中增添的虚构细节，其所引发的恐惧皆毫无二致。

===Original===

It was not consequences to happiness that troubled me, but something of far mightier scope, for I looked upon some little pulse of evil which, at its time, had seemed to die away in the thought, and lo, in all the years since then it had been ceaselessly waving onward in consecutive circles, whose outer rim touched and invaded the majestic symphony of unalterable principles of Beauty and Truth. Before the presence of that beholding there was no such thing as a little wrong in all the universe.
And now, in review, there passed before my mind all those paradoxes of being which, to our natural sense, forever perplex the relations between God and Man﻿—God, the omnipotent; Man, the free agent, the two concentric wheels of self-determining will which turn the universe. How can these things be?
In an instant I saw that hitherto unattainable How. Out of the depths of mystery it broke forth and stood in grand relief upon its midnight veil. Between truths there was no longer any jar; as on a map, illustrated by eternal light, I beheld all their relative bearings, and in the conviction of an intuition cried out, “True, true, divinely true!”
Do you ask me to give the process? As well might I attempt to define sight to a being born without eyes as to image, even to myself, at this moment, the mode of that apocalypse. Had memory of it as aught else than a fact remained to me, I had long since been consumed, as a red-hot needle dissolves away in oxygen. As it is, I remember not the manner, except that it was Sight; at the moment it was incommunicable by any human language. Yet the stamp of the intuition remains so indelibly upon my soul, that there is no self-evident truth which I could not more easily abjure than the undimmed and perfect harmony which, in that dreadful night, I beheld as an intuition.
After this I suffered hellish agonies, prolonged through an infinity of duration. As they were all embodied in symbols, I recall them but dimly, and the endeavor to relate them would be painful and profitless.
At the end of my representative road, arriving through growing distances, times, and tortures, God-drawn, I was hurried back to be launched forward in the direction of the other, the celestial tendency.

===Chinese===

困扰我的并非对幸福造成的后果，而是某种规模宏大得多的事物，因为我凝视着某丝微末的恶念脉动——在当时，它似乎已在念头中自生自灭，看哪！在此后的漫长岁月里，它却在连续不断的涟漪圆圈中永无休止地向外荡漾，其外缘触及并侵入了“美与真理”那不可更改之法则的壮丽交响。在目睹那一幕的临在之下，整个宇宙间根本不存在所谓“微不足道的过错”。

此刻，我脑海中回顾检视着所有那些在凡常感官看来永远困扰着人神关系的“存在之悖论”——上帝，全能的主宰；人，具有自由意志的主体；这两道驱动宇宙旋转的自主决断意志的同心之轮。这些事物何以能并存协调？

刹那间，我看清了此前无法企及的“何以如此”。它从神秘的幽深深处破土而出，赫然凸显在它那子夜般的面纱之上。真理与真理之间不再有任何龃龉冲突；宛若在一幅被永恒光芒映照的地图之上，我领悟到了它们之间所有的相对方位，在直觉的笃信中我高呼：“真实，真实，神圣之真实！”

你若问我其推演过程究竟为何？我此刻向自己描摹那场启示的形态，其艰难程度无异于试图向一个生来双目失明之人解释视觉究竟为何物。倘若留在心中的除却一个客观事实外还留有其他记忆，我恐怕早已被焚烧殆尽，宛若一根烧红的铁针在氧气中瞬间熔化消解。正因如此，除却知晓那是“洞见”之外，我记不得具体的方式；在当时，那绝非任何人类语言所能表达。然而那直觉的烙印在我的灵魂深处是如此不可磨灭，以至于即便抛弃任何不言自明的公理真理，我也绝不可能放弃在那个可怕的夜晚作为直觉所见证的澄澈无瑕、完美无缺的和谐法则。

此后，我承受了地狱般的酷刑折磨，持续了无尽的岁月长度。由于它们皆具象化为种种象征，我如今只依稀记得，试图叙述它们亦将痛苦且徒劳。

在那条象征之路的尽头，穿过不断延展的距离、岁月与折磨，在神圣力量的牵引下，我被匆匆带回，并被重新推向另一个方向——那个通往天界的轨道。

===Original===

The music of unimaginable harps grew clearer with every league of speed; symbols were turned to their most ravishing uses; the gleam of crystal gates and empyrean battlements flashed on me with increasing radiance; the sky breathed down a balm which signified love, love﻿—quenchless love. At the end of this journey I arrived also; and, between towers of light, was about to pass through into a land resounding with infinite choruses of joy. I was detained. Again the voice spake to me, “The thing is too great for thee; seek not to enter. As thou wast preserved at the end of thy former way from going into the fires to which it led, so also now do I guard thee from beholding the fatal glories of the Divine face to face.” With inconceivable grief I hid my face in my hands and returned, weeping bitterly.
At this moment, for the first time since coming from my room, I became aware of the external world. My friend still walked by my side, supporting me through the darkness. We had not come half a mile while I passed through all that awful vision!
Presently we came to a short bridge. Little conceiving the state of mind from which I had just emerged, Bob said to me, with the impression that the novelty of the idea would give me an attractive suggestion of adventure, “See the Styx.” Groaning in spirit, I looked down upon that dark and sullen water which rolled below me, and saw it mightily expanded beneath horrible shadows toward a shore which glowed with the fires of my earlier vision. “My God!” I cried, “am I again journeying toward the Infernal? Yes, it must be so; for even this man, who has learned nothing of my past tortures, knows and tells me this is one of the rivers of Hell!”
Bob caught a glimpse of the pain he had innocently caused me, and assured me, for the sake of my peace, that he had only been jesting. “This is not the Styx at all,” said he, “but only a small stream which runs through Schenectady.” By pointing out to me familiar surroundings, by persuasion, by entreaty, he at length prevailed upon me to cross the bridge; yet I only did so by concealing my eyes in his bosom and clasping his hands with the clutch of a vice.
Supposing that light and the restorative influence of wine would relieve me, he led me to a restaurant, and there, sitting down with me to a table, called for a glass of Port.

===Chinese===

随着飞掠的速度每跨越一里（league），难以想象的竖琴之声便愈发清澈；象征皆被赋予了最令人心驰神往的妙用；水晶之门与最高天城垛的微光以愈发灿烂的光辉在我眼前闪耀；苍穹向下吹拂着象征着爱、爱——无休无止之大爱的芬芳甘霖。在这段旅途的终点我也同样抵达了；在两座巍峨的光明之塔间，我正欲迈步跨入一片回荡着无尽欢乐合唱的乐土。我被拦住了。那个声音再次对我说道：“此事对你而言过于宏大；切莫试图踏入。正如你在前一条道路的尽头得以保全、未曾堕入它所通向的烈火那般，如今我也同样护佑着你，免得你与神圣威光面对面而招致毁灭。”怀着难以想象的悲痛，我用双手捂住面孔折返回来，痛哭流涕。

就在那一刻，自我走出房门以来，我头一次感知到了外部世界的存在。我的朋友依然走在我身旁，在黑暗中搀扶着我。当我历经整场可怕的异象之时，我们前行的距离竟还不满半英里！

不多时，我们来到了一座短桥前。鲍勃丝毫未曾料想到我方才走出的心境状态，满以为这一新奇的想法能给我带来引人入胜的冒险意味，便对我说：“瞧，斯提克斯河（Styx）。”在心灵的哀鸣中，我俯视着脚下翻滚奔流的阴沉黑暗水流，见它在可怖的阴影下浩荡膨胀，涌向一片被我先前异象中烈火所照亮的彼岸。“我的上帝啊！”我呼喊道，“难道我又要踏上通向阴曹地府的旅程了吗？是的，一定是这样；因为就连这个人，对我的过往折磨一无所知，却也知道并亲口告诉我这是地狱的河流之一！”

鲍勃隐约察觉到了他无心之失给我带来的痛苦，为了让我安心，他向我保证自己只是在开玩笑。“这根本不是什么斯提克斯河，”他说，“只不过是流经斯克内克塔迪（Schenectady）的一条小溪流罢了。”通过向我指出熟悉的周遭景物，经过一番劝导与哀求，他终于说服我穿过了桥梁；但我唯有将双眼深埋在他的怀中，双手如铁钳般紧紧攥住他的双手，才勉强迈步走过。

满以为灯光与葡萄酒的提神功效能缓解我的痛苦，他领着我走进了一家餐馆，并在那里同我围坐在一张桌旁，要了一杯波特酒（Port）。

===Original===

In the unnatural shadow which enwrapped all things and persons, a man was standing near the door, and in the conversation which he was carrying on with another I heard him use the word “damn.”
In an instant my mind, now exquisitely susceptible, took fire from that oath as tinder from steel. “There is, indeed,” I soliloquized, “such a thing as damnation, for I have seen it. Shall I be saved?” This dreadful question forced me to determine it with an imperative fascination. I continued. “Oh thou Angel of Destiny, in whose book all the names of the saved are written, I call on thee to open unto me the leaves!”
Hardly had I spoken when upon a sable pedestal of clouds the dread registrar sat before me, looking immeasurable pity from his superhuman eyes. Silently he stretched out to me the great volume of record, and with devouring eyes I scanned its pages, turning them over in a wild haste that did not preclude the most rigid scrutiny. Leaf after leaf flew back; from top to bottom I consumed them in my gaze of agony. Here and there I recognized a familiar name, but even my joy at such revelations took nothing from the cruelty of the suspense in which I looked to find my own. With a face cold as marble I came to the last page, and had not found it yet. Drops of torture beaded my brow as with eye and finger I ran down the final column. One, two, three﻿—I came to the bottom﻿—the last. It was not there!
My God! nothing but thine upbearing arms at that moment kept me from eternal annihilation. In stony horror I sat dumb.
After the Angel of Destiny took back his book and shut it with the echo of doom, I know not what time elapsed while I dwelt in that unfathomable abyss of despair. I saw Eternity, like a chariot out of which I had fallen, roll out of sight upon the bowed and smoking clouds to leave me, a creature of perdition, in an inanity of space and out of the successions of duration. Familiar faces were around me, yet the thought of obtaining relief from them never crossed my mind. They were powerless to help a sufferer of the immortal pangs.
If, as I sat at the table, a cauldron of boiling lead had been brought in and set beside me, I would have leaped into it with exultant haste, to divert my mind from spiritual to physical sufferings. Through a period which the hashish-eater alone can know, I sat speechlessly beside my friend.

===Chinese===

在笼罩着万物与众人的非自然阴影之中，一名男子正伫立在门旁，在他同另一人的交谈中，我听见他吐露出了“该死（damn）”这个词。

刹那间，我那此刻敏锐至极的心智，宛若火镰碰击燧石般从那句咒骂中瞬间燃起了烈火。“世间确实存在所谓的天谴沉沦（damnation），”我自言自语道，“因为我已亲眼目睹。我是否能得救呢？”这个可怕的问题以一种不可抗拒的魔力迫使我必须探明答案。我继续念道：“啊，执掌命运的天使啊，凡得救之人的姓名皆镌刻在你的书卷之上，我求你为我翻开那些书页！”

我话音未落，在乌云翻滚的墨黑基座上，那可畏的记录官赫然端坐在我眼前，超凡的双眸中流露出无尽的怜悯。他默默向我递出了那部宏大的巨册，我以饥渴吞噬般的眼神扫视着书页，在一阵狂乱的匆忙中翻阅着，却丝毫未曾遗漏最严苛的审视。一页又一页书页翻飞而过；我以痛楚的目光自上而下逐一审视。我不时认出某个熟悉的名字，然而即便目睹此等显现带来的欣喜，也丝毫未能减轻我在寻找自己名字时那份悬心未决的残酷折磨。我面色冷峻如大理石般翻到了最后一页，却仍未觅得踪迹。痛苦的汗珠如水滴般渗出额头，我的目光与手指顺着最后一栏逐一滑落。一、二、三——我触到了最底端——最后一人。那里没有我！

我的上帝啊！在那一刻，若非你那托举的双臂，我早已陷入永恒的毁灭消亡。我陷入石化的骇然之中，呆若木鸡。

待命运天使收回他的书卷、伴随着末日审判的回响将其阖上之后，在深不见底的绝望深渊之中，我不知究竟过去了多少时光。我望见“永恒”宛若一辆我从中摔落的战车，在低垂弥漫的硝烟云雾中滚滚远去，消失在视野之外，将我这个注定沉沦的受造物抛弃在虚无的虚空之中，脱离了岁月的连绵序幕。熟悉的面庞环绕在我身旁，但我心中从未闪过向他们求取慰藉的念头。面对承受着不朽剧痛的受难者，他们根本无能为力。

倘若当我围坐在餐桌旁时，有人端来一锅沸腾的滚铅置于我身侧，我定会带着狂喜的急迫纵身跃入其中，只为将心神从灵性的苦难转移至肉体的痛苦之中。在唯有食大麻者方能体会的漫长岁月里，我默默无言地坐在朋友身旁。

===Original===

Suddenly upon the opposite wall appeared a cross, and Christ the Merciful was nailed thereon. I sprang from my seat; I rushed toward him; I embraced his knees; I looked intensely into his face in voiceless entreaty. That sad face sweetly smiled upon me, and I saw that my unspoken prayer was granted. Through my soul, as through a porous film, swept a wind of balm, and left it clean. The voice that had attended me through all past journeyings, now changed from stern upbraiding to unimaginable love, spoke gently, “By the breath of the Spirit thine iniquities are borne utterly away.” To colossal agonies peace as great succeeded, and, thus sustained, I returned to my room.
Yet all my sufferings had not yet been fulfilled. The moment that I reached home I threw myself down upon my bed. Hardly had I touched it when, from all sides, devouring flame rolled upward and girt me in with a hemisphere of fire. Shrieking, I leaped up and ran to my friends, who cared for me till the wrathful hallucination was overpast.
At this day it seems to me almost incredible that I ever survived that experience at all. Yet, inexplicable as it may be, when I awoke the next morning, I was as free from all traces of suffering as if I had been, all the evening previous, cradled in a mother’s arms.

===Chinese===

突然间，在对面的墙壁上显现出一座十字架，仁慈的基督正被钉在其上。我从座位上一跃而起；我向他飞奔而去；我紧紧抱住他的双膝；在无声的哀求中我凝视着他的面容。那张悲悯的面孔向我报以温柔的微笑，我看出自己未曾吐露的祈求已蒙应允。一阵芬芳抚慰的和风拂过我的灵魂，宛如拂透一层透气的薄膜，使它重归洁净。在过往的所有旅程中一直伴随着我的那个声音，此刻已从严厉的斥责蜕变为难以想象的至爱，轻柔地说道：“借由圣灵的气息，你的罪愆已尽数除尽。”伴随着巨大的痛苦退去，同等浩瀚的平静降临于心，在这份力量的支撑下，我返回了自己的房间。

然而，我所受的折磨尚未彻底终结。一跨入房门，我便一头倒在床上。然而我的身躯才刚触及床榻，吞噬一切的烈焰便从四面八方腾空而起，化作一片炽热的半球将我团团围住。尖叫着，我跳起身奔向朋友们，他们悉心照料着我，直至这场狂暴的幻象彻底消退。

时至今日，我能从那场经历中侥幸生还，在我看来依然近乎难以置信。然而，无论多么不可思议，当我次日清晨苏醒之时，我身上的痛苦痕迹已荡然无存，仿佛前一整个夜晚，我始终安然依偎在母亲温暖的怀抱之中。

## XI The Book of Symbols / 第十一章 象征之书
===Original===

Of all experiences in the hashish state, my indoctrination into spiritual facts through means of symbols was the most wonderful to myself. In other visions I have reveled in more delicious beauty, and suffered horrors even more terrible; but in this I was lifted entirely out of the world of hitherto conceivable being, and invested with the power of beholding forms and modes of existence which, on earth, are impossible to be expressed, for the reason that no material emblems exist which even faintly foreshadow them.
Among men we communicate entirely by symbols. Upon any thought which has not its symbol in the Outer, “untransferable” is stamped indelibly. A certain relation between two thoughts is beheld by one human mind. How shall the man inform his neighbor of this relation? There is no meatus for it through any of the labyrinths of material sense; it can not be seen, heard, felt, smelt, or tasted. What is to be done?
A flock of cranes are assembling from the four quarters of heaven to hold their aerial council on some tall crag above him. Into our thinker’s mind flashes a bright idea. Those birds shall mediate for his relation a passage into his brother’s understanding. The cranes (*grues*) are coming together (*con*), and in this visible symbol he embodies his invisible relation, and the name henceforth that passes for it among men is “congruous.”
Yet there is one condition beyond the mere discovery of an apt symbol which is necessary before that symbol can be circulated as the banknote which bases its security on the intangible coin within the spiritual treasury. That coin must be universally felt to exist, or the bill will be good for nothing. In the present instance, the idea of the relation expressed by “congruous” must already have been perceived by the communicatee, or the communicator will be unable to express himself intelligibly. Rather should we say, the idea of the possibility of this relation must exist before the former can perceive it; for, if he recognizes such a possibility, then, by virtue of this very capacity, he will immediately actualize the possible, and on the communication of the symbol perceive the idea of “congruity,” though it be for the first time.
The question now arises, What state of mind lies back of, and conditions the capacity to recognize, through symbols, the mental phenomena of another?

===Chinese===

在处于大麻状态的所有经历中，通过象征手段对属灵真相的领悟，于我而言乃是最为奇妙的。在其他异象中，我曾沉醉于更为甜美怡人的瑰丽，亦曾遭受过更为骇人听闻的恐惧；但唯独在这一境界中，我被彻底超拔出此前所能想象的存在世界，被赋予了目睹各种存在形式与模式的能力——由于尘世间根本不存在哪怕能微弱预示它们的物质象征，这些形式与模式在世间根本无法言传。

在尘世凡人之间，我们完全依靠象征进行交流。任何在外部世界找不到对应象征的思想，皆被无可磨灭地盖上了“不可传递”的印记。某人心智中领悟到了两种思想之间的某种特定关联。他该如何将这种关联告知他的同伴？通过物质感官的重重迷宫，根本不存在传递它的通道；它无法被看见、听见、触摸、嗅闻或品尝。那该如何是好？

一群灰鹤从四方天际汇聚而来，在他头顶的高耸岩壁上召开它们的空中会议。我们的思想者脑海中灵光一闪。那些飞鸟将成为他思想关联的媒介，架起通往同胞理解的桥梁。灰鹤（*grues*）聚集在一起（*con*），他在这一有形的象征中具象化了他那无形的关联，此后在人类之间流传开来的名称便成了“相融相合（congruous）”。

然而，除却单纯发现一个恰当的象征之外，还存在着一个先决条件——唯有满足此条件，该象征方能如同钞票般流通，其信用建立在属灵金库中那无形硬币的基础之上。人们必须普世地感受到那枚硬币的存在，否则钞票便是一纸空文。在眼前的例子中，“相融相合”所表达的关联概念，必须已被接收信息者所感知，否则表达者将无法清晰明了地传递意图。更确切地说，该关联的可能性概念必须在前者感知它之前就已然存在；因为倘若他认可了这种可能性，那么凭借这一能力本身，他便会立即将可能化为现实，并在收到该象征后首次领悟到“一致契合”的概念。

此刻问题油然而生：究竟是何种心智状态，在背后支撑并决定了人们借由象征辨识他人精神现象的能力？

===Original===

Plainly this: the two who are in communication must be situated so nearly upon the same plane of thought that they behold the same truths and are affected by the same emotions. In proportion as this condition is violated will two men be unappreciating of each other’s inner states.
Now in hashish it is utterly violated. In the hashish-eater a virtual change of worlds has taken place, through the preternatural scope and activity of all his faculties. Truth has not become expanded, but his vision has grown telescopic; that which others see only as the dim nebula, or do not see at all, he looks into with a penetrating scrutiny which distance, to a great extent, can not evade. Where the luminous mist or the perfect void had been, he finds wondrous constellations of spiritual being, determines their bearings, and reads the law of their sublime harmony. To his neighbor in the natural state he turns to give expression to his visions, but finds that to him the symbols which convey the apocalypse to his own mind are meaningless, because, in our ordinary life, the thoughts which they convey have no existence; their two planes are utterly different.
This has not only occurred in my own case, but in several others﻿—in persons upon whom I have experimented with hashish. At their highest exaltation, so earnest has been the desire to communicate the burden which overpowered them, that they have spoken forth the symbols presented to their minds; yet from these symbols men around them, in the unexalted state, drew an entirely different significance from the true one, or, perceiving none at all, laughed at what was said as an absurdity, seeing nothing in the name of some ordinary thing or mode of being to excite such emotions of terror or ecstasy as were produced in the hashish-eater. Yet many a time, as I stood near, by these symbols thus expressed, have I been able to follow the ecstatic wanderer, and recognize the exact place in his journey at which he had arrived as something which I had once seen myself.
It is this process of symbolization which, in certain hashish states, gives every tree and house, every pebble and leaf, every footprint, feature, and gesture, a significance beyond mere matter or form, which possesses an inconceivable force of tortures or of happiness.

===Chinese===

答案显而易见：进行交流的双方必须处于极其相近的思想层面上，以至于他们能目睹相同的真理，并受到相同情感的触动。这一条件遭到破坏的程度有多大，两人对彼此内在状态的理解隔阂就会有多深。

而在大麻状态下，这一条件被彻底颠覆打破了。在食大麻者身上，由于其全部心智机能超自然的广度与活跃度，实际上已经发生了一场世界的更迭转换。真理并未膨胀，但他的视野却变得如同望远镜般深邃；他人眼中的微弱星云，抑或根本视而不见之物，他却能以洞穿一切的审视深入探寻，距离在极大程度上根本无法遮蔽其锋芒。在曾经只有发光迷雾或一片绝对虚无之处，他探寻到了由属灵存在构成的奇妙星座，测定了它们的方位，并破译了其崇高和谐的法则规律。他转向处于常态的同伴，试图诉说自己的异象，却发现向自己心智传递启示的那些象征，在对方眼中毫无意义——因为在我们的凡俗生活中，这些象征所传达的思想根本不复存在；两人的层面全然不同。

这种情况不仅发生在我自己身上，而且在其他若干人身上——那些我曾对其进行大麻实验的人身上——亦屡屡出现。在他们极度亢奋的高峰时刻，想要传达那压倒自己的重负的愿望是如此迫切，以至于他们大声喊出脑海中呈现的象征；然而对于这些象征，身旁处于常态的人们要么解读出了与本意风马牛不相及的含义，要么根本茫无所知，将所说之语当作荒诞不经的胡话一笑了之，在某种普通事物或存在模式的名号中，完全看不出究竟有何能激起食大麻者心中那般恐惧或狂喜的由头。然而不知有多少次，当我守候在侧时，通过这些如此表达出来的象征，我却能紧紧追随那位狂喜的漫游者，并将他旅途中所抵达的准确地点，认作我自己曾经目睹过的风景。

正是这种象征化的过程，在大麻的特定状态下，赋予了每一棵树木与房屋、每一粒卵石与树叶、每一道足迹、面容与姿态一种超越纯粹物质或形式的深刻意蕴，蕴含着无可想象的折磨或幸福的力量。

===Original===

Perhaps one of the most difficult things to convey to a mind not in the hashish delirium, by the symbols which there teach the manner of its process, or by any others, is the interchange of senses. The soul is sometimes plainly perceived to be but one in its own sensorium, while the body is understood to be all that so variously modifies impressions as to make them in the one instance smell, in another taste, another sight, and thus on, *ad finem*. Thus the hashish-eater knows what it is to be burned by *salt* fire, to *smell* colors, to *see* sounds, and, much more frequently, to *see* feelings. How often do I remember vibrating in the air over a floor bristling with red-hot needles, and, although I never supposed I came in contact with them, *feeling* the sensation of their frightful pungency through *sight* as distinctly as if they were entering my heart.
In the midst of sufferings unfathomable or raptures measureless, I often thought of St. Paul’s God-given trance, and the “*ἄρρητα ῥήματα ἃ οὐκ ἐξὸν ἀνθρώπῳ λαλῆσαι.*” Never was I more convinced of anything in my life than that our translation, “which it is not lawful for a man to utter,” is wholly inadequate. It should be, “which it is impossible to utter to man;” for this alone harmonizes with that state of intuition in which the words are “speechless words,” and the truths beheld have no symbol on earth which will embody them. Though far from believing that my own ecstasy, or that of any hashish-eater, has claim to such inspiration as an apostle’s, the states are still analogous in this respect, that they both share the nature of disembodiment, and the soul, in both, beholds realities of greater or less significance, such as may never be apprehended again out of the light of eternity.
There is one thought suggested by the symbolization of hashish which I can not refrain from introducing here. In some apocalyptic states of delirium like that which I have mentioned, and others succeeding it, there were symbols of an earthly nature used, which not only had never before conveyed to me such truth as I then saw, but never had expressed any truth at all. Things the least suspected of having any significance beyond their material agency were perceived to be the most startling illustrations and incarnations of spiritual facts.

===Chinese===

对于未曾陷入大麻谵妄的心智而言，通过在大麻中阐述其过程模式的那些象征或任何其他方式，最难传达的事情之一或许莫过于感官的通感互换。人们有时能清晰感知到，灵魂在自身的感受中枢里原本浑然一体，而唯有肉体才将各种印象进行形形色色的修饰转化，使其在一种情况下显现为嗅觉，在另一种情况下显现为味觉，抑或视觉，等等，直至无穷（*ad finem*）。因此，食大麻者深知被“咸”的火焰灼烧、“闻”到色彩、“看”到声音、以及更为频繁地“看”到感觉究竟是何体验。我无数次清晰记得自己悬浮震颤在铺满炽热铁针的地板上空，尽管我自知从未与它们触碰，却能通过“视觉”清晰“感知”到它们令人胆寒的刺痛，宛如它们正一根根刺入我的心脏。

在深不可测的苦难或无边无际的狂喜之中，我常常想起圣保罗蒙神所赐的沉醉出神状态，以及那些“隐秘的言语，是人不可说的（*ἄρρητα ῥήματα ἃ οὐκ ἐξὸν ἀνθρώπῳ λαλῆσαι*）”。我一生中从未比此刻更加笃信：我们通常所译的“是人不可说的”，完全未能达意。它应当被译为“是向人说不出的”；因为唯有这般解释，方能契合那种言语皆为“无言之言”、所目睹的真理在尘世间找不到任何象征得以具象的直觉境界。尽管我绝不敢妄称自己或任何食大麻者的狂喜能拥有使徒般的灵感启示，但这两种状态在脱离肉体这一本质上依然具有相通之处，且在二者之中，灵魂皆能目睹或大或小具有深远意义的真实，而这些真实除了在永恒的光芒中，可能再也无法被凡人所领悟。

由大麻的象征化所唤起的一丝思绪，令我在此忍不住要提上一笔。在某些宛如我曾提及的那类谵妄启示状态、以及随后的其他境遇中，所使用的尘世自然象征，不仅此前从未向我传递过我所见证的那般真理，甚至根本从未表达过任何真理。那些最不曾被怀疑拥有超越物质功用之意蕴的事物，却被感知为属灵真相最令人震惊的阐释与化身。

===Original===

Now where, among created things, shall we set the boundaries to this capacity for symbolizing. In view of that which I saw, especially upon the last detailed memorable night, I felt, and still feel, forced to the conclusion that there is no boundary. If, as the true philosopher must believe, the material was created for the spiritual, as the lower for the higher, the means for the end, it is impossible that any minutest lichen should exist as mere inert matter, lessonless to the soul of all creation’s viceroy﻿—man.
What a world of symbols, then, lie sleeping in expectancy of the approaching times which shall bring some translator to their now unnoticed sermons, and bid them speak of unconceived beauties and truths!
Following out the perverted tendencies of a pseudoscience, we are now forever seeking some reason for the existence of the outer world as it is, which will utterly insphere it within the ends of material well-being. Plainly perceiving that respect for the Creative Wisdom will not permit us to suppose that anything, however microscopic, has been made aimlessly, we belabor our brains with attempts to find out some physical good arising out of the being of every object in all the terrene kingdoms. Such a thing was created that man might be cured of the headache; such another, that his food might be varied; still another, that a convenient circulating medium might be in his power. Doubtless such corporeal goods were among the final causes of some portions of the creation. Our trouble is not our activity in the discovery of these, but that beyond their petty circle discovery does not dare to set her foot, for fear of being called visionary.
Doubtless, when God has lent us a tenement to lodge in, albeit for a few days and nights, it is our duty to find out and apply all those materials of repair which will keep it in good order till we pass finally from its low doorstep into our palace; as honorable tenants, and for the sake of our own better preservation, this is both duty and right, so far as it may be done without bespangling and frescoing our wayfaring house as if it were to be our perpetual home. Yet what effort can be meaner, what more unworthy of Spirit, than studiously to degrade the whole sublime Kosmos into one colossal eating-house, wardrobe, or doctor’s shop for the body?

===Chinese===

那么，在所有的受造物之中，我们究竟应当在何处为这种象征能力划定界限呢？鉴于我所目睹的一切，尤其是在那场详述过的难忘之夜里，我感到、且至今仍深信不得不得出这样的结论：根本不存在任何界限。倘若如真正的哲学家所必定坚信的那样，物质乃是为精神而造，正如低等之物为了高等之物、手段为了目的而存在，那么任何哪怕最微小的地衣，都绝无可能仅作为惰性物质而存在，而对作为一切造化之总督——人类的灵魂——毫无教益启示。

那么，究竟有多少象征的世界正沉睡在期待之中，等待着未来的时光为它们如今被忽视的无声布道带来一位翻译者，并命它们倾诉出未曾构想的美丽与真理啊！

追随着伪科学那误入歧途的倾向，我们如今总在为外部世界的客观存在寻找某种理由，企图将其完全圈禁在物质福祉的狭隘目的之内。人们清晰意识到，对“造物智慧”的敬畏绝不允许我们假设世间有任何事物——哪怕细微至极——是漫无目的造就的，于是我们便绞尽脑汁，试图从大地万象的存在之中找出某种物理益处。这种事物的创造是为了治愈人类的头痛；另一种事物的存在是为了丰富人类的饮食；还有一种事物则是为了让人类掌握便捷的流通媒介。毫无疑问，此类肉体层面的益处确属某些受造物最终原因的一部分。我们所面临的症结，并不在于热衷于发现这些益处，而在于探索的脚步绝不敢踏出它们狭隘的圈子半步，生怕被扣上耽于空想的帽子。

诚然，当上帝借给我们一处寓所栖身——哪怕仅有短短几日几夜——去寻找并施用所有修缮材料以使其保持良好状态，直至我们最终越过低矮的门槛步入宫殿，乃是我们的职责所在；作为体面的住客，为了自身更好的保全，这既是责任也是权利，只要我们在打理时，不至于将这处暂居的客栈点缀修饰得宛若我们要永居此间的家园一般。然而，还有何种行径能比处心积虑地将整座崇高的宇宙贬低为一处专供肉体享用的巨型饭馆、衣帽间或药铺，更为卑劣鄙俗，更为玷污神圣的灵性呢？

===Original===

Good men, perhaps wise men, are forever looking for something medicinal in the scorpion, or edible in the fungus, to vindicate God’s claim to an intention. Most ingeniously do they fabricate supposititious purposes for outer things; hopelessly writhe in the folds of perplexity when, with all their far-fetched hypotheses, they can not see what material good is to come out of some obstinate resistant to their analysis.
Blind philosophers! Nature refuses to cramp herself within your impossible law; she rejects your generalization; she throws off the shackles of your theory! For the sake of mere physical well-being, it had doubtless been far better that never a centipede had been created; that the most formidable snake had been the harmless garter; that the euphorbus had never put forth a leaf, nor the seleniuret a vapor. Yet this is not the grand aim of the system of things, but that man might, for the present, have symbols for the communication of manly thought; that God himself, for the future, might have symbols for the revelation of Divine Truth, when, in the grand unveiling, rocks, trees, and rivers﻿—yea, the smallest atom also﻿—shall come thronging up along all the ways of the Universe to unseal their long-embosomed messages, and join in the choral dance of the Spiritual﻿—the only science﻿—to the Orpheus-music of the awakened soul.

===Chinese===

善良的人们——或许还是明智的人们——总在蝎子体内寻找药用价值，或在真菌之中寻觅食用可能，以此来为上帝的造物用意辩护。他们极其巧妙地为外在事物捏造出种种虚构的目的；而当用尽所有牵强附会的假说，仍无法看破某种顽固抗拒其分析的事物究竟能产生何种物质益处时，他们便绝望地深陷在困惑的泥潭中挣扎。

盲目的哲人啊！大自然拒绝将自身禁锢在你们那些行不通的狭隘法则之中；她摒弃你们的归纳概括；她甩脱你们理论的枷锁桎梏！若仅仅为了肉体的福祉，世间无疑根本就不该创造任何百足蜈蚣；最凶猛的毒蛇也该是无害的袜带蛇；大戟属植物（euphorbus）根本不该长出一片叶子，硒化物（seleniuret）亦不该散发出半点蒸气。然而，这绝非事物体系的宏大目标——造化的真正目的，乃是让当下的人类拥有传达崇高思想的象征；让上帝自己在未来拥有揭示“神圣真理”的象征，届时在宏大的终极揭示之中，岩石、树木与江河——乃至最微小的原子——皆将沿着宇宙的所有途程蜂拥汇聚，开启它们深藏已久的心声密旨，伴随着觉醒灵魂所奏响的奥菲斯（Orpheus）神乐，共同融入这唯一的终极科学——“精神”的齐声合唱曼舞之中。

## XII Today, Zeus; Tomorrow, Prometheus / 第十二章 今日宙斯，明日普罗米修斯
===Original===

At what precise time in my experience I began to doubt the drug being, with me, so much a mere experiment as a fascinating indulgence, I do not now recollect. It may be that the fact of its ascendency *gradually* dawned upon me; but, at any rate, whenever the suspicion became definite, I dismissed it by so varying the manner of the enjoyment as to persuade myself that it was experimental still.
I had walked, talked, and dreamed under the hashish influence; I would now listen to music and see acting, that, under such circumstances, I might note the varying phenomena, if any occurred.
To reach New York for the purpose I would go by water, sailing down the glorious Hudson under the full moon; and this would still be another opportunity for experimenting.
Upon one of the largest and most beautiful of the steamers which ever glided down the shining pathway of the river upon a moonlight night of summer, I stepped, at eight o’clock in the evening, accompanied by several of my friends, and carrying in my pocket a box of boluses. The gangplank was drawn in, and we were on our way.
In the few moments which elapsed before the steward appeared, brandishing his noisy harbinger of things edible, I managed to swallow, unseen, a number of the spheroids contained in my box.
On regaining the deck from that savory, subaqueous cavern where, amid sepulchral lights, five hundred Americans of us had, for the incredible space of fifteen minutes, been fiercely elbowing each other in insane haste to secure that grand national end, indigestion, we found the broad disk of the moon just above the horizon, and, on armchairs taken forward, sat down, with our toes thrust into the bulwark-netting, for our post-cœnatial smoke. Cigars and studently habits of thinking impelled us toward song, and for two hours, at least, the low rocks which skirt the upper channel echoed with “There is music in the air,” “Co-ca-che-lunk,” and other collegiate harmonies.

===Chinese===

在我的经历中，我究竟从哪个确切的时刻开始怀疑这种药物于我而言与其说是单纯的实验，倒不如说是一种令人沉沦的诱惑，如今我已记不真切了。或许是它对我精神的主导地位在*不知不觉中*逐渐显现；但无论如何，每当这种怀疑变得明确起来，我便通过变换享受的方式将其驱散，以此自欺欺人地坚信这仍旧是在做实验。

此前我曾在大麻的影响下漫步、交谈与做梦；而如今我打算去聆听音乐、观赏戏剧，以便在此类情境下记录下各种不同的现象（假若确有发生的话）。

为了达成这一目的前往纽约，我决定走水路，在满月的光辉下泛舟顺着壮丽的哈德逊河（Hudson）顺流而下；而这亦将成为又一次实验的良机。

在一个夏日的月夜里，我登上了曾在这条河流波光粼粼的水道上滑行过的规模最大、最为华美的轮船之一。时值晚间八点，我由几位密友陪同，口袋里装着一盒大麻药丸。跳板收起，我们便启程出发了。

在乘务员手摇喧闹的餐铃宣告开饭之前的短短片刻里，我设法避开众人的目光，悄悄吞下了盒中的数粒药丸。

在那间弥漫着诱人香气的“水下洞穴”里，在阴森昏暗的灯光下，我们五百名美国人为了达成“消化不良”这一宏大的国民目标，在不可思议的十五分钟内疯狂地推搡拥挤；随后我们重返甲板，只见一轮硕大的明月正悬于地平线上方，我们坐在搬到前甲板的扶手椅上，将脚趾伸入舷墙绳网中，开始享受餐后的吞云吐雾。雪茄与书生的思维习惯驱使我们引吭高歌，在至少两个钟头里，环绕着上游航道的低矮礁石间回荡着《空气中弥漫着仙乐》（There is music in the air）、《柯-卡-切-隆克》（Co-ca-che-lunk）以及其他大学校园的和声乐曲。

===Original===

The Opera, with its glory of lights, passionate song, orchestral crashes, and scenery, whirls the soul on with it, indeed, in a bewildering dance of delight; the ballad we love, sung feelingly by the woman we love, at that hour when to lift the curtains would only let in more twilight, is a calm rapture which is good for the heart; if it be not too near, the bugle discourses rich melody and spirit-stirring among the mountains of its birth; yet, beyond all other music, grant me a song trolled from manly throats, which keep good chord and time, and first learned within those homely walls which, to the true American collegian, are dearer than all the towers of Oxford.
Reverend Union! it is not thine to deck thyself in the outworn trappings of feudal pomp; not even is it thine to bear upon thy brow the wrinkles of unnumbered years, though long before thou lackest such prestige its sign shall come upon thee. Thou hast no high places for lineage nor fat tables for gold; thou art beautiful neither in marble nor carved workmanship. Yet art thou the mother of thinkers and workers﻿—high souls and brave hearts, which make their throb felt in the giant pulses of a great nation. To these Gracchi of thine dost thou point and say, “Behold my jewels.” With the love of thy sons thou art crowned more royally than turrets might crown thee; and better than all the remembrance of coronets upon thy calendar and ermine in thy halls is the thought that, grasping thy protectress hand, merit hath so often struggled up to fame out of the oblivion of namelessness and the clutch of poverty.
It is in the American college, with its freedom from fictitious distinctions, its rejection of all odious badges, which set genealogy and money over mind and heart; its inculcation of manly self-government rather than the fear of tyrannic espionage; its unrestrained intercourse between congenial souls, and its grouping of congenialities by society bonds, that the most perfect development of the social and individual man takes place. Here it is that, by attrition of minds, unworthy eccentricities are rubbed off, while the personal and characteristic nature of the man is solidified and polished into higher symmetry.

===Chinese===

歌剧凭借其璀璨的灯火、激昂的唱腔、管弦乐的轰鸣与布景，确实能将灵魂裹挟进一场令人目眩神迷的欢欣狂舞之中；而在暮色初降、拉开窗帘只会引来更多幽光的时刻，由我们心爱的女子深情吟唱的心爱民谣，亦是一种有益心神的恬静狂喜；号角若在不太近的地方吹响，亦能于其诞生的崇山峻岭间奏出丰沛悠扬、振奋人心的旋律；然而，在所有音乐之上，请赐我一首由男子汉宽厚的喉咙中唱出的歌谣——声调谐和，节拍严整，且最初是在那些对于真正的美国大学生而言比牛津的所有高塔都更为亲切朴素的四壁之内所习得的。
