令人景仰的联合学院（Union College）！你无须披戴封建繁文缛节的陈腐行头；甚至也无须在额头刻下无尽岁月的沧桑皱纹，尽管早在你缺失这份声望之前其荣光便已降临于你。你没有留给名门望族的显赫席位，亦无迎合万贯家财的奢华筵席；无论是在大理石的雕琢还是精巧的工艺中，你皆不以奢华为美。然而，你却是思想家与实干家的摇篮——那些高尚的灵魂与勇敢的心灵，令这伟大国度的雄浑脉搏为之震颤共鸣。你指着你的这群“格拉古兄弟（Gracchi）”骄傲宣称：“瞧，这便是我的珠宝。”在你学子们的爱戴之下，你所获得的冠冕比任何城楼堡垒所能赋予的都要崇高；而比起在你的校历上铭刻王冠徽章、在你的殿堂内铺设白鼬皮裘的一切荣耀，更为崇高的念头是：正是紧握着你守护之手的臂膀，诸多才俊得以一次次从无名的沉沦与贫困的桎梏中奋力挣脱，最终攀上声名显赫的高峰。

正是在美国的大学校园里——凭借其摒弃虚妄的阶层区隔，拒绝将门第与财富置于思想与心灵之上的可憎标签；凭借其倡导男儿的自我管理而非对暴虐窥探的畏惧；凭借志趣相投的心灵之间毫无拘束的交往，以及通过社团纽带将志同道合者紧密相连——社会与个体的健全发展才得以完满实现。正是在这里，通过思想的相互砥砺磨合，无谓的怪癖得以被磨平，而一个人独特鲜明的个性本质则得以巩固并被淬炼成更为高阶的对称与和谐。

===Original===

And here, last, though far from least, among all the true purposes of education, the heart gets its due in the attainment of those unworldly associations which, many a year after the actual presences which they symbolize have dropped down into the “long ago,” send up the hallowed savor of friendship and disinterestedness through the dust and cobwebs which choke well-nigh every other memory.
It is not wonderful that, out of such free and intimate converse among young men as we find in our colleges, song should spring up as a most legitimate and accredited progeny. He who should collect the college carols of our country, or, at least, those of them whose spice would not be wholly lost in the transplantation from their original time and place, would be adding no mean department to the national literature. Piquant, fresh-imaged, outwelling, and sitting snug to their airs, they are frequently both excellent poetry and music. Whether they ring through the free air of a balmy summer evening from a row of sitters on a terrace or a green, who snatch fragrant puffs of old Virginia between staves, or gladden a college room through the long evenings of winter, they are always inspiriting, always heart-blending, and always, I may add, well sung.
I have rambled round the complete circle of my digression to the place where I left my friends seated upon the forward deck and singing in the incipient moonlight. By the time that we had grown tired of singing, the river was very beautiful with the clear reflection of the sky, turning the spray of our prow to silver beads, and giving still snowier lustre to our wake. The excitement of music had put off that of hashish, but I was not surprised to feel the well-known thrill as our voices died away.
In a moment I became the fairy monarch. Etherealized and beautified, I was gliding upon my will-borne pleasure-vessel through the moonlit kingdom over which I was supreme. Now whippoorwills chanted me a plaintive welcome from the dreamy, wooded shores; fireflies illuminated, with triumphal lights, their palace fronts among the shadowy elms; and the little moon-glorified islands, that caught our waves upon their foreheads, sent back a delicious voice of laud and joy.

===Chinese===

正是在这里，在所有真正的教育旨趣之中，心灵最终获得了属于它的那份超脱凡俗的情谊——在这些情谊所象征的真实音容笑貌早已沉入“遥远的过往”许多年之后，依然能穿透几乎窒息了一切其他记忆的尘土与蛛网，升腾起友谊与无私的圣洁馨香。

年轻人在我们的大学里享有如此自由亲密的交往，歌声作为一种最合乎常理、最无可置疑的产物在此间蓬勃萌发，自然不足为奇。倘若有人能搜集整理我们国家的大学歌谣——或者至少是那些在脱离了最初的时空背景后其神韵仍未彻底丧失的作品——必将为国家文学增添一门不容小觑的门类。这些歌谣风趣俏皮、意象清新、喷涌自然，与曲调严丝合缝，往往兼具卓越的诗意与音乐性。无论是在芬芳夏夜的清新空气中，从坐在露台或草坪上、在乐句间歇处惬意抽着弗吉尼亚老烟草的成排学子口中响彻四周，还是在漫长的冬夜里为大学寝室带来欢声笑语，它们总是振奋人心，总是让心与心交融，而且我还想补充一句，总是唱得那般悦耳动听。

我这番迂回漫谈终于绕回了最初离开朋友们的原点——当时他们正坐在前甲板上，在初升的月色中引吭高歌。待我们唱累之时，江面上清晰倒映着苍穹的倒影，显得格外静谧绮丽，将我们船头溅起的浪花化作颗颗银珠，为船尾犁出的航迹赋予了胜雪的银辉。音乐的亢奋暂时压制了大麻的作用，但随着歌声渐歇，感受到那股熟悉的战栗悸动再次袭来，我丝毫未感意外。

刹那间，我化身为了仙境君王。在超凡脱俗与极尽升华之中，我乘坐着全凭心意驱动的游船，滑行在由我至高统治的月光国度之上。此刻，夜鹰从如梦似幻的茂密森林岸边向我吟唱起幽怨凄美的欢迎之歌；萤火虫在阴翳的榆树间用凯旋的华灯点亮了它们的宫殿门楼；而那些被月光镀上荣光的小岛，用它们的前额迎接着我们的波浪，送回了一阵阵欢愉与赞颂的美妙天籁。

===Original===

In this ecstasy I sat reviewing my domain until the moon stood at the zenith, and then pacing through the long saloons, I reveled in the ownership of gorgeous tapestry and panelings, and from the galleries looked proudly upon my retinue of beautiful women and brave men who sat or walked below.
When I shut my eyes I dwelt in a delicious land of dreams. Charging at the head of ever-victorious legions, I drove millions of laughing foes in playful rout through an illimitable field of roses. Down the mountains of Congo a whole universe of lithe and shapely negro children ran leaping, with their arms full of elephants’ tusks, boxes of gold-dust, and fresh coconuts, to be the purveyors of my palace. On the wings of a speechless music I floated through the air, and in the cloud-valleys played hide-and-seek with meteors.
A little after midnight I felt the hashish effects decreasing, and not having yet recognized that law of the drug which forbids prolonging its dreams by a second dose (nor, indeed, did I recognize it until several bitter experiences had taught me), I took five grains more.
Gradually more and more the hashish influence wore off. I went to my stateroom, and now, perfectly restored to the natural state, lay down, and all night slept quietly.
Upon awaking with the early sunlight I found that we were midway past the Palisades. Upon the eastern bank of the river the signs of suburban life had become visible in terraces, lawns, and verandas, and bells were audible down the bay.
It was not until we reached the pier that I felt the effect of my last bolus. I stepped ashore, and, for the first time, separated for a season from my fellow-voyagers. The morning already gave most earnest promise of a day which was to be one of the hottest of summer, and as I walked up that unsheltered quay alone, and with the sun streaming full upon me, I suddenly felt my heart catch fire. There was no premonitory, no mystery, no thrill; and this gave a more terrible tone to my suffering, for I burned among acknowledged and familiar realities without the possibility of remembering any former state of a calmer nature upon which to steady myself.

===Chinese===

在这份狂喜之中，我坐观巡视着我的领地，直至明月高悬天顶；随后我踱步穿过一间间漫长的沙龙大厅，陶醉在坐拥华美织锦与护墙板壁的占有欲中，从长廊上傲然俯瞰着坐在下方或漫步其中的端庄女眷与英武部从。

阖上双眸，我便栖身于一片甜美惬意的梦境之乡。我率领着百战百胜的军团冲锋陷阵，在无边无际的玫瑰花海中，将数以百万计含笑的敌手嬉戏般驱散溃逃。顺着刚果的崇山峻岭，整个宇宙间体态轻盈匀称的黑人孩童蹦跳着奔跑而下，怀抱满满的象牙、装满金砂的木箱与新鲜的椰子，充当我宫廷的御用贡使。在无声仙乐的双翼之上，我在半空中轻盈飞翔，在云雾幽谷中与流星捉迷藏。

午夜刚过不久，我感到大麻的效力正在减退，由于当时尚未领会那种严禁通过追加第二剂量来延长梦境的药物法则（事实上，直到数次切肤之痛的严酷经历后我才真正明白），我便又吞下了五格令。

大麻的影响逐渐消退殆尽。我走回自己的特等舱，此刻已彻底恢复常态，躺下身来，整夜酣睡得十分安稳。

迎着初升的晨光苏醒时，我发现轮船已行至哈德逊河西岸悬崖帕利塞兹（Palisades）的半途。在河流东岸，郊野生活的景象已在梯田、草坪与阳台门廊上清晰可见，海湾下游亦能隐约听见钟声回荡。

直到我们靠岸抵达码头，我才感受到最后一剂药丸的药力爆发。我迈步走上岸，头一次与旅伴们暂时分道扬镳。清晨的天色已然显露出这将是盛夏最酷热的一天的严酷迹象，当我独自一人走在那座毫无遮阴的码头上、迎着倾泻而下的灼热阳光时，我突然感到自己的心脏猛烈地燃烧了起来。没有任何先兆，没有神秘感，亦无半点战栗；这反而给我的痛苦平添了一种更为恐怖的基调，因为我是在公认且熟悉的现实之中经受烈火煎熬，根本无法忆起任何昔日更为宁静平和的状态来稳固自己的心神。

===Original===

Most fully did I then realize the hell of Eblis and its inextinguishable pangs, as, walking through the thronged streets of the great city, I laid my hand upon my heart to hide its writhings, and saw in every face of the vast multitude who hurried past tokens of something despairing and diabolic. The well-known long rows of palatial shops and gaudy windows swept by me as I paced along. The hurrying crowds of men upon the pavement who went to their businesses, and the fluctuating stream of carriages and omnibuses which rolled down the street, seemed, in their mere matter, nothing unusual to me. Yet the spirit which pervaded all things was that of the infernal. I wandered through a colossal city of hell, where all men were pursuing their earthly tendencies amid pomp and affluence as great as ever, yet stamped upon their foreheads with the dreadful sign of all hope of better things forever lost.
At all times the thoroughfare of a large town is a wilderness to me. In desert loneliness, on mountain tops, or by the side of an unfrequented stream, there is no such hermit conceivable as the hermit of a crowd. The study of character in faces, of universal human nature in its elbowings and windings toward its aim, may be pursued upon a city’s pave to the greatest advantage; yet overtopping all the external aspects of society found there is the solitude which inspheres the wanderer within himself, as he perceives not one being within the distance of miles to whom he is bound by any dearer interest than our common humanity.
But at this time how singularly, how especially was I a hermit! Still conscious of retaining some of the attributes of a man, I was surrounded by infernal forms and features, shaped, indeed, like my own, but with the goodwill, the hope, the confidence of our common life forever evaporated from them. Every one of the beings that hastened by me in hum and tumult looked under his eyebrows, with dreadful superstition, at his neighbor and at me. The ideal of hell, where all faith hath perished, and in endless mutation of couples the wretched sneer and glare at each other continually, was realized in that scene.
I could not bear the pavement, and so stepped into an omnibus, that I might behold less of that terrible ebb and flow of Life in Death.

===Chinese===

在穿行于这座大都市熙熙攘攘的街道上时，我最真切地体会到了易卜劣斯（Eblis）的地狱及其难以熄灭的酷刑折磨；我用手按住胸口试图掩饰心脏的抽搐痉挛，并在匆匆掠过的汹涌人潮的每一张面孔上，看到了某种绝望与魔鬼般的狰狞神色。当我步履沉重地前行时，那一排排熟悉的宫殿般奢华的商铺与俗丽的橱窗从我身侧掠过。人行道上忙于各自营生的匆匆人群，以及街上川流不息、摇晃颠簸的马车与公共马车长龙，纯就物质表象而言，对我来说并无任何异常之处。然而弥漫于万物之中的精神，却是完完全全的九幽地府。我漫步穿行于一座地狱般的巨型都市之中，在那里，所有的人皆在一如既往的奢华与富足中追逐着尘世的欲念，但在他们的额头上，却无一例外地烙印着一切美好希望已彻底荡然无存的可怖印记。

无论在任何时候，一座大城镇的通衢大道于我而言皆是一片荒野。在荒漠的孤寂中、在崇山之巅、抑或在罕有人至的溪流畔，世间根本想象不出任何隐士能比身处人潮之中的隐士更为孤独。在城市的人行道上，对人们面孔中性格的研究、对普世人性在向着自身目标推搡周旋过程中的观察，固然能发挥到极致；然而凌驾于在此所能发现的一切社会外在表象之上的，乃是将漂泊者自我禁锢于内心的无边孤寂——因为他意识到，在方圆数英里之内，根本找不到任何一个能借由比共同的人性更为亲密的情谊与他相连的存在。

然而在此时此刻，我成了何等奇特、何等彻头彻尾的隐士！我仍清醒地意识到自己保留着人性的某些特质，周遭却被来自地狱的形貌所环绕——他们的身形面貌固然与我相仿，但我们共同生活中的善意、希望与信任，却已从他们身上彻底蒸发殆尽。在喧嚣混乱中匆匆擦肩而过的每一个生灵，皆带着可怕的猜忌与迷信，从眉毛底下斜睨着身旁的同伴和我。信仰尽数毁灭、在永无休止的结对更迭中可怜的人们不断对彼此嗤之以鼻与横眉冷对的地狱图景，在那幕场景中得到了全然的再现。

我再也无法忍受这人行道上的折磨，于是跨进了一辆公共马车，以便能尽量少看到一些那在死境中痛苦沉浮的骇人生死之潮。

===Original===

As we rolled heavily over the stones of the street, I felt my heart transferred to some flinty roadbed, a fathom below the surface, where it writhed beneath the jar of wheels, and the puncture of the cruel rock-fragments yet communicated all its sufferings to me by slender cords of conduction, whose elastic fibre stretched more and more as we rode on, and grew tenser with an unutterable pain. At the same time, all my fellow-passengers in the omnibus seemed staring at me with hot and searching eyes; in one corner I cowered from their glance, and sat with my hand upon my face. They whispered; it was myself of whom they talked, and I distinctly heard them use the word “hashish.”
I got out of the omnibus and again took the pavement, realizing that there was nowhere any relief for my pangs. It would be vain to detail all the horrors through which I passed before I took shelter in the house of a friend. Among them not the least were a heart on fire, a brain pierced by a multitude of revolving augers, and the return, amid dim inner flames, of the fearful symbolization and the demon-songs of former visions.
Arriving at my friend’s, I pleaded fatigue, and lay down. Hours were wretchedly passed in falling asleep, and then darting up in terror at some ideal danger. Sometimes a gnashing maniac looked at me, face to face, out of the darkness; sometimes into rayless caverns I fell from the very heavens; sometimes the lofty houses of an unknown city were toppling over my head in the agonies of an earthquake. Agonies, I say, for their throes seemed like human sufferings.
Out of this woe I emerged entirely by noon, but began to be aware that I should never again, in the hashish state, be secure in the certainty of unclouded visions. The cup had been so often mingled, that its savor of bitterness would never wholly pass away. Yet ascribing all the pain which, in this instance, I had endured, to some unfavorable state of the body (I had not yet realized the law of a second dose), I supposed that, by preserving a general healthy tone of the system, hashish might be used harmlessly.

===Chinese===

当我们沉重地碾过街道上的石块时，我感到自己的心脏被移置到了地下一英寻（fathom，约一点八米）深的坚硬碎石路基之下，在那里，它在车轮的颠簸震颤与残酷碎石的刺扎下痛苦扭曲，却又通过细弱的传导神经将所有痛苦悉数传递给我，随着车轮前行，那富有弹性的纤维越拉越长，在难以言状的剧痛中绷得越来越紧。与此同时，公共马车里的所有乘客似乎都在用灼热而探究的目光死死盯着我；我瑟缩在一个角落里躲避他们的目光，用手捂住面孔颓然坐着。他们交头接耳窃窃私语；他们谈论的正是关于我，而且我清晰地听见他们吐露出了“大麻”这个字眼。

我逃出公共马车，重新走上了人行道，深知世间没有任何地方能化解我的剧痛。在逃入一位朋友家中避难之前，若要细述我所经历的所有恐怖，皆是徒劳。在那些恐怖之中，不仅有被烈火焚烧的心脏、被无数旋转钻头钻刺的大脑，更有在前次异象中那可怕的象征化景象与群魔狂歌在暗淡内火中的重现。

抵达朋友家后，我借口疲惫不堪躺了下来。随后的几个钟头在昏睡与惊醒的折磨中苦苦度过，时常因某种假想的危险而惊恐地一跃而起。有时一个咬牙切齿的疯子在黑暗中与我面面相对、死死瞪视；有时我从天穹顶端径直坠入暗无天日的幽深洞窟；有时一座未知高楼林立的城市在地震的剧痛抽搐中朝我的头顶轰然倾塌。我说那是剧痛抽搐，因为它们坍塌的挣扎宛若人类的痛苦呻吟。

直到正午时分，我才彻底从这场厄运中脱身，但我开始清醒地意识到，在大麻的状态下，我将再也无法确保拥有毫无阴霾的澄澈异象了。杯中的苦汁已被搅动得太过频繁，其苦涩的滋味将永远无法彻底消散。然而，由于将此次所承受的所有痛苦归咎于身体的某种不佳状态（我当时尚未意识到追加第二剂量的法则），我依然误以为：只要维持机体整体的健康基调，大麻仍可无害享用。

## XIII *Idola Theatri* and the Prince of Whales / 第十三章 剧场假象与鲸鱼王子
===Original===

Waiting until the next day at evening I took a moderate bolus, say twenty-five grains, and repaired to the theatre.
In the action of the pieces which were performed I lived as really as I had ever lived in the world. With the fortunes of a certain adventurer in one of the plays my mind so thoroughly associated me that, when he was led to the block and the headsman stood over him, I nerved myself for the final stroke, and waited to feel the steel crash through my own neck. He was reprieved, and, in his redemption, it was I who exulted.
The effect of some rich-toned frescoing above the stage was to make me imagine myself in heaven. Yet “imagine” is not the proper word, for it does not express the cloudless conviction of reality which characterized this vision. There were no longer any forms or faces visible below me, but out of the wondrous rosy perspective of the upper paneling angels came gliding, as through corridors hewn of ruby, and showered down rays of music, which were also beheld as rays of color.
A most singular phenomenon occurred while I was intently listening to the orchestra. Singular, because it seems one of the most striking illustrations I have ever known of the preternatural activity of sense in the hashish state, and in an analytic direction.
Seated side by side in the middle of the orchestra played two violinists. That they were playing the same part was evident from their perfect uniformity in bowing; their bows, through the whole piece, rose and fell simultaneously, keeping exactly parallel. A chorus of wind and stringed instruments pealed on both sides of them, and the symphony was as perfect as possible; yet, amid all that harmonious blending, I was able to detect which note came from one violin and which from the other as distinctly as if the violinists had been playing at the distance of a hundred feet apart, and with no other instruments discoursing near them.
According to a law of hashish already mentioned, a very ludicrous hallucination came in to relieve the mind from its tense state. Just as the rapture of music, lights, and acting began to grow painful from excess, I felt myself losing all human proportions, and, spinning up to a tremendous height, became Cleopatra’s Needle.
A man once remarked to young Dumas, “My poor friend, M. Thibadeau, returned home, took off his spectacles, and died.”

===Chinese===

等到次日傍晚，我服下了一剂适度的大麻药丸，大约二十五格令（grain，约一点六克），随后前往剧场。

在舞台上演绎的戏剧剧情中，我宛如真切生活在世间一般身临其境。对于其中一出戏里某位冒险家的命运，我的心智与他产生了如此深切的共情，以至于当他被押上断头台、刽子手高高伫立在他上方时，我全身紧绷等待着承受最后的致命一击，静候着感受利刃劈裂我自己脖颈的剧痛。然而他蒙受了赦免，在那份死里逃生的解脱中，狂喜欢呼的人正是我自己。

舞台上方某些色调华贵的壁画所产生的效果，让我误以为自己置身于天堂之中。然而“以为”并非恰当的词汇，因为这根本无法表达那一异象所特有的毫无阴霾的真实确信感。在我下方，再也看不见任何凡人的形貌或面庞，而从上方护墙板那奇妙的玫瑰色纵深之中，天使们宛若穿行在红宝石雕琢而成的长廊中般翩跹滑过，倾泻下一道道音乐的光芒，而在我眼中，那音乐亦同样显现为一道道斑斓的色彩。

当我全神贯注聆听管弦乐团演奏时，发生了一种最为奇异的现象。之所以说它奇异，是因为这似乎是我所知晓的大麻状态下感官超常敏锐、且朝向分析性方向发展的最惊人例证之一。

在乐团正中央，两位小提琴手并肩坐着演奏。从他们运弓的整齐划一来看，他们显然在拉奏同一个声部；在整首乐曲中，他们的琴弓同时起伏，保持着绝对的平行。在他们两侧，管乐与弦乐齐声轰鸣，交响乐和谐得无懈可击；然而，在所有那般和谐融洽的交织之中，我却能无比清晰地分辨出哪个音符出自哪一把小提琴，其清晰程度宛若两位乐手相隔一百英尺演奏、且周遭没有任何其他乐器发声一般。

依照前文已述及的大麻法则，一场极其滑稽的幻觉适时降临，将心智从高度紧绷的状态中解脱出来。正当音乐、灯光与表演的狂喜因过度而开始化为痛苦之时，我感到自己失去了所有人类的躯体比例，旋转着升腾至惊人的高空，变成了“克利奥帕特拉方尖碑”（Cleopatra’s Needle）。

曾有人对小仲马（young Dumas）说道：“我可怜的朋友蒂巴多先生（M. Thibadeau）回到家中，摘下眼镜，然后就去世了。”

===Original===

“Did he take off his spectacles first?” asked Dumas. “Yes, truly,” replied the other; “but why?” “Merely how delightful it must have been to be spared the grief of seeing himself die!”
About as absurd a duality as that inferred for Monsieur Thibadeau, if he had kept on his spectacles, was the duality with which I looked up and saw my own head some hundred feet in the air. Suddenly a hashish-voice rang clearly in my ear, “Sit still upon thy base, Eternal Obelisk!” Ah me! I had not realized till then how necessary it was that I should preserve the centre of gravity. What if I should go over? One motion on this side or that, and dire destruction would overwhelm the whole parquette. From my lofty top I looked down upon responsible fathers of families; innocent children; young maidens, with the first peach-bloom of womanhood upon their cheeks; young men, with the firelight of ambitious enterprise new-kindled in their eyes. There was absolutely no effort which I was unwilling to make to save them from destruction. So I said to myself, “Be a good obelisk and behave yourself, old fellow; keep your equilibrium, I entreat you. You don’t want to clothe all the families of New York in mourning, I know you don’t; control yourself, I beg.” Bold upright and motionless I sat, until it pleased the gods to alter my shape, most opportunely, when I was just giving out, to the far more secure pyramid, after which all hallucinations gently passed away.
The hashish state, in its intensest forms, is generally one of the wildest insanity. By this I do not mean to say that the hashish-eater at such a season necessarily loses his self-control, or wanders among the incoherent dreams of a lawless fancy, for neither of these propositions is true. As I have heretofore remarked, self-government during the delirium, from being at first apprehended as a necessity, grows up at length into a habit, and the visions that appear before the shut or open eyes of the ecstatic have an orderly progress and a consistent law according to which they are informed, which elevate them above the prodigal though meaningless displays of fancy into the highest sphere of imagination.

===Chinese===

“他是先摘下眼镜的吗？”仲马问道。“确凿无疑，”对方答道，“可这有什么干系？”“只是想想就觉得惬意，他竟能免受亲眼看着自己死去的悲伤啊！”

倘若蒂巴多先生一直戴着眼镜便会产生的荒诞二重身，恰好同我此刻抬头望见自己脑袋高悬在百英尺高空中的二重身一样荒谬可笑。突然间，一声大麻唤起的嗓音在我耳畔清晰回荡：“坐稳在你的基座上，永恒的方尖碑！”哀哉！直到那一刻我才意识到，自己保持重心是何等生死攸关之事。假若我倒下去了该如何是好？往这边或那边稍有倾斜，灭顶之灾便会席卷整个正厅池座（parquette）！从我高耸的顶端，我俯瞰着下方肩负重任的一家之主、天真烂漫的孩童、双颊初绽少女桃红娇晕的妙龄女子、以及眼中新燃起雄图抱负烈火的青年才俊。为了拯救他们免遭毁灭，世间绝对没有任何努力是我所不愿付出的。于是我对自己说道：“做一座温驯的方尖碑，老老实实守好规矩，老兄；求你务必稳住平衡。你绝不想让整个纽约的家庭都披麻戴孝，我知道你绝不想；求你克制住自己。”我笔直端坐，纹丝不动，直到诸神眷顾，恰逢我即将支撑不住之际，极其适时地将我的形态转化为稳固得多的金字塔，随后所有的幻觉才温柔地消散殆尽。

大麻状态在其最强烈的形式下，通常呈现为一种极度疯狂的癫狂。我这番话并非指食大麻者在此类时刻必然会丧失自我克制力，抑或迷失于肆无忌惮的狂乱幻想之中，因为这两种论断皆非事实。正如我此前所指出的，在谵妄期间的自我控制，从起初被视作一种必要，最终成长为一种习惯；而在狂喜者闭合或睁开的双眸前所显现的异象，皆遵循着一种有条不紊的演进与前后一致的规律法则，这赋予了它们超越异想天开却毫无意义的浮夸展示，使其升华为最高维度的想象力境界。

===Original===

Yet, after all, there are reasons for calling the state an insanity, and a wild insanity, which will defend the name to all who can realize them from a description, and far more completely to those who have known them by experiment.
In the first place, when self-control has reached its utmost development, and the tortured or exultant spirit restrains itself from all eruptive paroxysms of communication among those people to whom its secret would be unwisely imparted, there is still a sense of perfect passivity to some Titanic force of life, which, for good or evil, must work on through its seeming eternity. Hurried through sublimest ascending paths, or whirled downward through ever-blackening infinitudes, longing for a Lemnos where the limbs may rebound from solid ground, even though shattered by the shock, there is no relief for the soul but to endure, to wait, and through a time of patience but faintly imaged by the nine days of the headlong Hephaestus. When the Afreet who was of old your servant becomes your lord, he is as deaf to petitions as you were avaricious in your demand for splendors.
Again: at the moment of the most rapturous exultation, the soul hears the outcry of the physical nature pouring up to its height of vision out of the walls of flesh, and the burden of that cry is, “I am in pain; I am finite, though thou art infinite!” The cords which bind the two mysterious portions of our duality together have been stretched to their ultimate tensity, and the body, for the sake of its own existence, calls the soul back into the husk which it can not carry with it. Oftentimes, in the presence of the most ravishing views, have I felt these cords pulling me downward with as distinct a sensation as if they were real sinews, and, compelled to ask the question “Is this happiness or torture?” soul and body have returned opposite verdicts.
These two facts constitute hashish a most tremendous form of insanity.
At intervals, however, in the enchanted life which I led under the influence of the drug, there occurred seasons of a quieter nature than the ravishment of delirium, when my mind, with a calm power of insight, penetrated into some of its own kingdoms, whose external boundaries only it had known before, reflected, marveled, and took notes as serenely as a philosophic voyager.

===Chinese===

然而归根结底，将其称为一种癫狂、一种狂野的癫狂，依然是有充分理由的——对于凡能从文字描述中体悟其境的人而言，这一命名完全站得住脚；而对于那些曾借由亲身实验知晓它的人来说，更是如此。

首先，当自控力达到极致，饱受折磨或欣喜若狂的心灵在那些不可轻率吐露秘密的人群中，竭力抑制住任何想要宣泄交流的突发冲动时，却仍会感受到对某种泰坦巨人之力的彻底顺从——无论是福是祸，这股力量都必将在其看似漫长的永恒中持续运转。或在崇高至极的攀升之途上飞掠狂奔，或在愈发漆黑的无垠深渊中旋转直坠，渴望能寻得一处如利姆诺斯岛（Lemnos）般的坚实着陆点，哪怕四肢会在撞击中粉身碎骨，心灵也别无解脱之道，唯有忍耐、等待，熬过一段赫菲斯托斯（Hephaestus）坠落九天九夜都难以充分比拟的漫长苦熬。昔日曾是你仆役的恶魔（Afreet），一旦翻身成为你的主宰，他对你的哀求便会置若罔闻，正宛若当初你对奇观的贪得无厌一般无情。

再者：在极尽狂喜的高潮时刻，灵魂会听见肉体本性自血肉的壁垒深处、向着那高不可攀的异象之巅发出的凄厉呼号，那哭喊的主旨便是：“我痛苦不堪；我是有限的，而你却是无限的！”维系我们二元存在那两个神秘部分的绳索已被绷紧到了极致，肉体为了自身的存活，拼命呼唤灵魂退回它无法带走的躯壳之中。不知有多少次，在目睹最为勾魂摄魄的奇景之际，我感受到这些绳索将我向下拽拉，其触感清晰得宛如真实的筋腱，迫使我不由发问：“这究竟是至福还是酷刑？”而灵魂与肉体给出的裁决却截然相反。

正是这两大事实，构成了大麻作为一种最为骇人听闻的癫狂形式的明证。

然而，在药物影响下我所度过的那段梦幻生活中，偶尔也会出现比谵妄的狂喜更为平静祥和的时段；每当此时，我的心智便凭借一股宁静的洞察力，深入到自身的某些王国内部——此前它仅知晓其外部边界——宛若一位哲思漫游者那般，安然地沉思、惊叹并记录心得。

===Original===

In the department of philological discovery I sometimes reveled for hours, coming upon clues to the geneaology of words and unexpected affinities between languages, which, upon afterward recalling them (though only in a few cases was I able to do this), I generally found substantiated by the authorities of science, or, if they had not before been perceived by any writers who I had at hand, at least bearing the stamp of a strong probability of correctness.
I mention but one of them as with me merely conjectural, for it bases its plausibility upon a root in the Sanskrit, with which language I do not pretend to be acquainted.
I remembered during one of these calm, suggestive states that the Latin *cano* (to sing) and *candeo* (to shine) were supposed to derive their origin from a common Sanskrit root, whose signification was “to dart forth, as the sun his rays of light.” The thought struck me, Might not other vocal utterance than singing be found cognate with the out-darting of light also? I would see. The Latin “*fari*,” “to speak,” referred me back to its Greek equivalent, *φάναι*. The verb “to shine” was “*φαίνειν*.” So far, in sound at least, the two were affiliated. It now occurred to me that “*φως*” was both “a light” and “man,” in his prerogative of speech, with a slight variation of accent in the different cases. I had here four words (dividing the last by its two meanings), all of whose original roots must have been something very nearly like *φα-*. On referring to authorities, I found that the fountainhead of the Greek “*φαίνω*” is supposed to be the Sanskrit “*bhâ*,” “to shine forth.” Following out the result of my previous argument, I connected all the words with this root, and in this conjecture saw both light and speech as effluences from Brahm, the great giver of all radiance, and man not merely an effluence from him, but, in virtue of speech, a “shiner” also, a reflector of Him from whose radiance he came, and into whose glory he should be absorbed. Now all this process (be its result true or false) was accomplished internally in a hundredth part of the time which it will take to read it﻿—nay, almost instantaneously, and with a sense of delight in the mental activity which carried it on such as the creation of his highest ideal by an artist gives him when he stands mute before his marble.

===Chinese===

在语言学发现的领地里，我有时会长达数小时流连忘返，不断探寻到字词谱系的线索以及不同语言之间出人意料的亲缘关联；事后回想起来（尽管我仅在少数情况下能做到这点），我通常发现它们得到了科学权威的证实；即便在我手头作家的著述中此前未曾被察觉，至少也带有极大概率具备正确性的印记。

我在此仅提及其中一例，于我而言这纯属推测，因其合理性建立在梵语词根的基础之上，而我并不自诩通晓这门语言。

在其中一种平静且富于启发的境况下，我记起拉丁语中的 *cano*（歌唱）与 *candeo*（发光），被认为源自同一个梵语词根，其本义为“喷薄而出，宛若太阳射出万道光芒”。我脑海中猛然闪过一个念头：除歌唱之外，其他发声言语是否亦能与光芒的喷薄发现同源关联呢？我倒要看个究竟。拉丁语中的“*fari*”（说话），指引我回溯至其希腊语对应词 *φάναι*。而动词“发光”则是“*φαίνειν*”。到目前为止，至少在读音上，二者血脉相通。此时我忽然想到，“*φως*”既可表示“光芒”，又可凭借人类说话的特权而表示“人”，仅在不同格位中的重音略有差异。至此我手头有了四个词（将最后一个词按其双重含义拆分），它们最初的词根必定皆与 *φα-* 极其近似。在查阅权威文献后，我发现希腊语“*φαίνω*”的源头被认为是梵语中的“*bhâ*”，意为“照耀发光”。顺着我先前论证的结论，我将所有这些词汇皆同这一词根联系在一起，在这番推想中，我将光芒与言语皆视作来自大梵（Brahm）——一切光辉的伟大赋予者——的流溢，而人类不仅是自他而来的流溢，更凭借说话的能力而同样成为一名“发光者”，成为映照出赋予他光芒、且他终将融归其荣光的那位至高者的反射之镜。这一切思维过程（无论其结论是真是假）在内心完成所耗费的时间，不及阅读它所需时间的百分之一——不，近乎是在瞬息之间便已告成，伴随着一种对推动这一思辨的心智活动的由衷欣悦，宛如一位艺术家在用大理石塑造出自己至高理想后、默然伫立在雕像前的陶醉之情。

===Original===

Another field through which I sometimes wandered was sown with those sound-relations between words which constitute the pun. For hours I walked aching with laughter in this land of Paronomasia, where the whole Dictionary had arrayed itself in strophe and antistrophe, and was dancing a ludicrous chorus of quirk and quibble. If Hood had been there, the notes which he would have taken had supplied him with materials for the Comic Annuals of a cycle. Rarely did the music of a deeper wit intermingle with the rattling fantasies of the pun-country; never was anything but the broad laugh heard there, and the very atmosphere was crazy with oxygen. Were it possible to transport to a country such as this those grave professors of the moralities who have been convicted of contempt of the court of Mirth, and high treason to the King of Misrule, how delightful would it be to behold their iron diaphragms vibrating perforce, and the stereotyped downward curves at the corners of their mouths reversed until they encroached upon the boundaries of their juiceless cheeks! But, in hashish states, temperament and previous habit so much decide tendencies, that the transport-ship which bore these convicts would float inevitably to the mouth of Acheron, or strand midway upon some reef upraised by a million of zoophytic Duns Scotuses.
Out of the number of double-entendres which appeared to me (and they probably amounted to thousands), I recollect but very few. To recall them all would be nearly, if not quite as difficult as to remember the characteristics of each separate wheat-head in a large harvest-field after having but once passed through it. I give two of them.
A youth, not at all of that description which “maketh a glad father,” was seen standing at the counter of a gaudy restaurant. Glass after glass of various exhilarating compounds was handed to him by the man in waiting, and as quickly drained. I did not observe that the genius of decanters received any compensation for the liquors consumed from the young man who demanded them, and modestly asked him how he had been induced to purvey to the drinker’s thirst on so liberal a scale. With arms akimbo, and casting upon me a most impressive look, the official replied, “Like the man in Thanatopsis, I am

> “ ‘Sustained by an unfaltering *trust*.’ ”

===Chinese===

我有时漫步的另一片领域，则播撒着构成双关语的词汇之间的语音关联。在这一“双关之国（Paronomasia）”中，我常常笑得前仰后合走上几个钟头，在那里，整部词典把自己编排成正旋舞歌与反旋舞歌，跳着由妙语与俏皮话交织而成的滑稽合唱之舞。倘若托马斯·胡德（Hood）曾身临此境，他所记下的札记足以供他编纂一个时代周期的《幽默年刊》（*Comic Annuals*）。更深层次的睿智乐章极少混杂在双关语国度那噼啪作响的幻想之中；在那里所能听闻的无非是开怀大笑，连整片空气中都因富氧而近乎狂乱。倘若能将那些被判藐视“欢乐法庭”、对“狂欢之王”犯下大不敬之罪的道貌岸然的道德学教授们流放至这样的国度，看着他们铁石般的横膈膜被迫剧烈震颤，嘴角那刻板下垂的弧度被彻底翻转、直至侵入枯瘪面颊的边缘，那该是何等痛快的景象！然而，在大麻状态下，人的性情与过往习性在极大程度上决定了倾向，运载这些囚徒的流放船不可避免地会漂向阿刻戎河（Acheron）的入口，或搁浅在由百万植虫般的邓斯·司各脱（Duns Scotuses）堆筑起的暗礁中途。

在向我显现的无数双关语中（其数量恐有数千之多），我如今能忆起的寥寥无几。若要将它们尽数唤醒，其难度即便不亚于、也几乎等同于在仅穿行过一次广袤的麦田之后，便要记住每一支麦穗的独特特征。在此我仅举两例。

一名全然不属于那种“使父亲欢喜”的年轻人，被发现伫立在一间俗丽餐馆的柜台前。侍者将一杯又一杯各种提神调配的饮品递到他手中，随即被他一饮而尽。我并未注意到这位支配酒瓶的精灵从那位索要酒水的年轻人手中获得过任何分文报酬，便谦逊地询问侍者究竟是何缘由驱使他以如此慷慨的规模满足这位饮者的干渴。侍者双手叉腰，向我投来极其意味深长的一瞥，回答道：“如同《死亡默想》（*Thanatopsis*）中的那个人一样，我

> ‘依靠着毫不动摇的信任（trust，亦指赊账）维系。’”

===Original===

Upon the steps of the post-office stood another young man, who had been disappointed in a remittance from the parental treasury. “What are you doing there?” I asked. “I am waiting patiently until my *change* come.”
Occasionally there intervened between the vagaries of pun and double-entendre some display of comic points in human nature, which were as amusing as the puns themselves. For instance, I remember the representation to me of a man of remarkable self-esteem, who happened, as he sat in my presence, to appease the irritation of his scalp with his digit. Just then a peal of thunder shook the sky above us. “Heavens!” cried our friend, “to think that it should thunder because a man scratched his head!”
I feel that these things lose very much of their original effervescence in the relation, for at the time they none of them seemed so much told to me as acted before me; nor was it the action of a stage, but of a vivified picture, where fun, in all its myriad mutations, was embodied to sight, and the joke was as much apprehended by the eye as by the feeling. Every gesture of the figures that passed before me told more of raillery than tongue could utter, and it was this fact that sometimes made pantomime upon the stage a perfect feast of mirth to me as I sat seeing it in the appreciative state induced by hashish. At such seasons, not the faintest stroke of humor in look or manner escaped me, and I no doubt often committed that most gross error in any man, laughing when my neighbors saw fit not to be moved.
At one time, in my ramble through the realm of incongruities, I came to the strand of the Mediterranean, and beheld an acquaintance of mine standing close beside the water. With a tourist’s knapsack upon his back, and a stout umbrella in his hand, to serve the double purpose of a walking-stick, he drew near and accosted me. “Will you go with me,” said he, “to make a call upon a certain old and valued friend?”
“Most willingly, if you will let me know his name.”
“It is the Prophet Jonah, who still occupies submarine lodgings in a situation, to be sure, rather cold and damp, yet commanding a fine water privilege.” “There is nothing,” I replied, “which would please me more; but how is it to be accomplished?” “Be patient, and you shall see.”

===Chinese===

在邮局的台阶上，伫立着另一名因未能从父母的金库中如愿获得汇款而沮丧不已的年轻人。“你在这儿做什么呢？”我问道。“我正耐心地等待我的转机（change，亦指零钱）到来呢。”

偶尔，在双关语与双关妙趣的奇思怪想之间，也会穿插展示一些人性的滑稽特质，其逗趣程度丝毫不逊于双关语本身。譬如，我记得曾向我展现过一位自尊心极强的人，当他恰好坐在我面前时，用手指去挠头皮以止痒。就在那一刹那，一道惊雷震颤了我们头顶的天空。“天哪！”我们的朋友惊呼道，“想想看，一个人挠了挠脑袋，天上竟然就打雷了！”

我深知这些轶事在转述之中会丧失极多最初的沸腾活力，因为在当时，它们在我看来绝非转述于我听，而是活生生地在我面前上演；那亦非舞台上的表演，而是一幅被赋予了生命的生动图景，滑稽在千变万化之中尽显于眼前，笑料被眼睛捕获的程度丝毫不亚于心灵的感受。从我面前走过的人物，其每一个姿态所传达的讽刺意味皆非舌簧所能尽述，正是这一事实，使得当我在大麻诱发的敏锐鉴赏状态下端坐观赏舞台哑剧时，它成了一场不折不扣的欢乐盛宴。在那些时刻，眼神或举止中最微弱的幽默笔触也休想逃过我的法眼，毫无疑问，我经常犯下凡人所能犯下的最粗鄙的失态——在邻座众人认为不宜动容时开怀大笑。

有一次，在我漫步穿越这片光怪陆离的荒诞领域时，我来到了地中海的海岸边，见我的一位熟人正伫立在水边。他背上背着旅行者的双肩包，手中握着一把兼作拐杖的结实雨伞，走上前来同我打招呼。“你愿同我一起去走一遭吗，”他说，“去拜访一位珍贵的昔日老友？”

“万分乐意，只要你告诉我他的大名。”

“那便是先知约拿（Jonah），他如今仍栖身于水下寓所之中，虽说环境相当阴冷潮湿，倒也坐拥极佳的水景特权。”“世间没有什么能比这更合我心意了，”我回答道，“可这该如何实现呢？”“耐心点，你马上便能看到。”

===Original===

Just then a slight ripple ridged the surface of the sea, bubbles appeared, and then there followed them the black muzzle of Leviathan, who, with mighty strokes, pushed toward the shore. Arriving there, his under jaw slid halfway up the beach, and his upper jaw slowly rose like a trapdoor, disclosing a fearful chasm of darkness within. I looked down the throat of the beast, and beheld descending it a rickety wooden staircase, which was evidently the only feasible access to interior apartments. Hardly would I have dared to trust myself to the tumbledown passage but for the importunate hand of my companion, which pressed me along beside him through the doorway and down the steps. The monster let down his grisly portcullis behind us, and in total darkness we groped to the bottom of our way, where we emerged into the most shabby room that ever dawned upon the eyes of the visiting committee of a benevolent association.
The central figure was an unutterably lean and woebegone looking man, who, on a rush-bottomed chair, the only one in the room, sat mending his sole pair of unmentionables by the aid of a small needle-book which I was informed his mother had given him on leaving home.
“Mr. Jonah, Mr. Fitz-Gerald,” said my friend, sententiously. “Very happy to know Mr. Fitz-Gerald,” returned the seer; though, as I took his lank and ghostly fingers in mine, he looked the very antipodes of happy. Decayed gentleman as he was, he shuffled around to do the honors of his mansion, and offered us the chair in which he had been sitting. We refused to dispossess him, and took our seats upon the shaky pine table, which, with one battered brazen candlestick, holding an inch of semi-luminous tallow, and a dog’s-eared copy of Watt’s Hymns, also a gift from his mother, completed his inventory of furniture.
“How do you like your situation?” asked my friend.
“Leaky,” replied Jonah; “find the climate don’t agree with me. I often wish I hadn’t come.”
“Can’t you leave here when you want to? I should think you would clear out if you find it uncomfortable,” said I to our entertainer.
“I have repeatedly asked my landlord to make out his bill and let me go,” replied the gentleman; “but he isn’t used to casting up his prophets, and I don’t know when I *shall* get off.”

===Chinese===

正在此时，海面上泛起了一道微弱的波纹，水泡汩汩冒出，紧接着利维坦（Leviathan）黑色的吻部露出了水面，伴随着雄浑的划水动作直奔岸边而来。抵达水畔后，他的下颌半搭在沙滩上，上颌如活板门般缓缓抬起，露出了内部黑黢黢的可怖深渊。我探头望向这头巨兽的喉咙，只见顺着咽喉向下延伸着一段摇摇晃晃的木楼梯，显然这是通往内部居所唯一可行的通道。若非同伴那不由分说的手紧紧拽着我同他并肩穿过门洞、顺阶而下，我绝不敢轻易踏入这条破败不堪的通道。巨兽在我们身后放下了那狰狞的落闸门，在伸手不见五指的漆黑中，我们摸索着走完了全程，来到了一间慈善协会探访委员会眼中所曾见过最为寒酸破败的房间里。

屋里的核心人物是一个骨瘦嶙峋、面容悲苦得无可言状的男子，他坐在屋中唯一的一把草编坐垫椅子上，正借助一本据说临别家门时母亲赠送的小针线包，缝补着他仅有的一条不可名状的裤子。

“约拿先生，这位是菲茨杰拉德先生（Mr. Fitz-Gerald），”我的朋友简明扼要地引见道。“非常荣幸结识菲茨杰拉德先生，”先知回应道；然而，当我将他干瘪如幽灵般的手指握在掌心时，他看起来却完全是幸福的对立面。尽管落魄潦倒，他依然尽力尽地主之谊，步履蹒跚地在屋里周旋，并将自己方才坐着的椅子让给我们。我们婉拒夺人所好，在摇摇晃晃的松木桌上坐了下来——这张木桌连同一盏装着一英寸微弱牛脂蜡烛的破旧铜烛台、以及一本被卷了毛边的《沃茨赞美诗集》（*Watt’s Hymns*，亦是他母亲所赠之物），便构成了他全部的家具家当。

“您觉得这里的居所如何？”我的朋友问道。

“漏水得厉害，”约拿答道，“发觉这里的气候同我不大合得来。我常常后悔当初真不该来。”

“难道您不能想走就走吗？要是觉得住着不舒服，依我看您早该搬出去了才是，”我对我们的东道主说道。

“我已经多次请求房东结清账单放我走人了，”这位绅士回答道，“但他不习惯吐出他的先知（casting up his prophets，谐音‘结算利润’profits），我不知道自己究竟何时才能脱身。”

===Original===

Just then Leviathan, from the top of the stairs, by a strange introversion looked down into his own interiors, and in a hoarse voice called out to know whether we were going to stay all night, as he wanted to put down the shutters.
“Be happy to give you a bed, gentlemen, but I sleep on the floor myself,” woefully murmured the poor seer. “You mustn’t neglect to call on me if you ever pass through Joppa, and﻿—and﻿—I ever get back myself.” We wrung Jonah’s hand convulsively, rattled up the crazy stairs, and ran out upon the sand just as Leviathan was about shoving off into deep water.
It may, perhaps, be hard to conceive how this incongruous element of the hashish visions should comport with all I have said upon the subject of those delicious raptures of beauty and sublime revealings of truth which break upon the mind under the influence of the drug.
How, it will be asked, as oftentimes it has been asked me already, can you put any confidence in discoveries of unsupposed significancy in outer things, and wonderful laws of mental being, attained during the hashish state, when you have also beheld vagaries of fancy which Reason instantly pronounced absurd? You do not believe that you really saw Jonah; how, then, can you believe that you saw truth?
I would answer thus: The domains of intuition and those of a wild fancy were always, in my visions, separated from each other by a clearly-defined and recognized boundary. The congruous and the incongruous might alternate, but they never blended. The light which illustrated the one was as different from that shed upon the other as a zenith sun is from lamplight. Moreover, at the time of each specific envisionment, I beheld which faculty of mind was working as distinctly as in the simplest tests of his laboratory the chemist knows whether cobalt or litmus is producing a certain change of color. The conviction of truth in the one case was like that of an axiom; in the other, such only as is drawn inferentially from mere sense.
We very little realize in our daily life that there are two species of conviction felt at various times by every man, yet a moment’s reflection will show that it is so.

===Chinese===

正在此时，利维坦从楼梯顶端凭借一种奇异的内视审视着自己的腹膛内脏，用沙哑粗粝的声音高喊着询问我们是否打算通宵留宿，因为他正准备放下百叶窗打烊了。

“若能为诸位绅士提供床铺我将不胜欣喜，但我自己都是睡在甲板地板上的，”可怜的先知凄楚地喃喃自语道，“倘若诸位有朝一日路过约帕（Joppa），而且——而且——我自己也能重返人间的话，切莫忘了前来看望我啊。”我们死死握了握约拿的手，骨碌碌跑上摇摇欲坠的楼梯，奔出落在沙滩之上，恰逢利维坦正要推水驶向深海。

对于大麻异象中这般光怪陆离的荒诞元素，何以能同我在该药物影响下心智所破晓的那些瑰丽绝伦的狂喜与崇高神圣的真理启示和谐共存，或许委实令人难以想象。

人们会发问，正宛如时常有人向我提出过的那样：既然你在大麻状态下亦目睹了理性立即宣判为荒谬绝伦的狂乱幻想，你又怎能对外部事物中那些前所未有的深刻意蕴、以及心智存在的奇妙法则抱有丝毫信任呢？你并不相信自己真的见到了约拿；既然如此，你又怎能相信自己目睹了真理？

我当如是回答：在我的异象之中，直觉的领地与狂乱幻想的领地始终被一条清晰界定且被充分辨识的界线彼此隔开。合理与荒诞或许会交替上演，但绝不会彼此混淆。照亮前者的光芒同洒落于后者的光辉之间的悬殊，恰如正午顶空的烈日之于微弱的灯火。更有甚者，在每一次特定的凝视时刻，我能清晰觉察到究竟是哪一种心智机能在发挥作用，正宛如化学家在其实验室最简单的试剂检验中，深知究竟是钴还是石蕊正在引发特定的颜色变幻。在前一种情况下对真理的确信宛若对公理的笃定；而在后一种情况下，则仅仅是源自单纯感官的推论断定。

我们在日常生活中极少意识到，每个人在不同时刻都会体会到两种截然不同的确信形式，然而片刻的沉思便能昭示事实确凿如此。

===Original===

I look, for example, at a piece of silk, and pronounce it black; if I were now to turn away without any further inspection, I should not be at all astonished to hear afterward, from someone who had examined the fabric more closely and in a better light, that it was not black, as I had pronounced it, but a dark shade of blue. I would be very willing to abjure my previous conviction, and, in this willingness, would show that I ascribed no absolute infallibility to the proofs of sense. Yet if the same man should assure me that the silk was both wholly black and wholly blue at the same time, I should instantly reject his assertion as absurd, for the reason that it was a violation of the very law of possibility. There would be no need of going back to test his truth, for it is denied by an entirely different conviction from that of sense﻿—the conviction arising from an insight into necessary and universal law.
Between the convictions of reality in the different hashish states, the boundary-lines are drawn even more distinctly than in the natural; and not only so, but the hashish-eater beholds those lines and acknowledges them, as the ordinary observer never does, from the fact that the practical wants of life make it convenient, nay, even imperative, that the data of sense should be treated as valid for the basis of action. We have neither time nor power in our present day-labor to secure the same unerring verdict upon objects of sense which the axiom gives us upon objects of intuition.
Nor is it necessary that in this life such a power should be possible. In a former part of these pages we have suggested a reason why it would not be best for the soul, thus early in its career, to have its intuitional domain enlarged. We may here, by another process, get at some of the final causes why this domain is just so large as it is. We have a sufficient scope of intuition for all our earthly purposes. Those truths are imparted to us as axioms which are necessary for the shaping of our habitual conduct.

===Chinese===

在数学公理前所作出的裁决，不仅在程度上与我们因外部客观事物所产生的确信完全不同，而且在本质上亦大相径庭。在一种情况下，确信源自精神——源于对必然且普适法则的洞见；而在另一种情况下，确信则源自感官。

在大麻的不同状态下，关于真实性的确信之间的分界线，比在常态下划定得更为清晰分明；不仅如此，食大麻者能够目睹并承认这些分界线，而寻常观察者却绝难做到——这是因为生活的实际需求使得将感官数据视作行动基石不仅极为便利、甚至势在必行。在日常的辛劳劳作中，我们既没有时间也没有精力去对感官对象寻求像公理赋予直觉对象那样毫无偏差的断言裁决。

在此生之中，具备这般能力亦绝无必要。在本书的前文之中，我们曾探讨过何以在灵魂历程的这般早期阶段扩大其直觉领域并非上策的缘由。在此我们亦可通过另一种推演，探寻出何以该领域恰好拥有如今这般规模的某些终极动因。对于我们所有的尘世目的而言，我们所拥有的直觉范围已然绰绰有余。那些作为公理传授给我们的真理，对于塑造我们日常习惯性的行为规范而言是必不可少的。

===Original===

In the thousands of constantly-recurring cases where, to direct our course wisely, it is necessary to know that a straight line is the shortest distance between two given points, that the whole is equal to its parts, and numerous similar facts, it would greatly hinder action were it necessary to take the rule and the balance into each specific consideration, and make a measurement according to sense. These truths, therefore, stand before every man in a light which shows them to be universal and necessary; they are everywhere assented to upon their mere statement. The animal is not God’s grand laborer, but man’s; *he*, therefore, needs no such faculty as intuition, the work of his little day requiring neither dispatch nor accuracy; and when he is impressed for human uses into the harness or the mill, the intuitions of his master guide him through the rein and the halter.
Doubtless, as our field of action widens, our intuitional eyesight shall be increased also; not only because otherwise we should be mortified and saddened by our purblindness and the sense of making no progress proportional to the pace of our circumstances, but because God will never leave the workmen of his purposes hampered in their action among the colossal plans of the eternal building.
There is one more fact to which I would advert in this rather rambling portion of my narrative, which characterizes the hashish state at times when it does not reach the height of delirium. I refer to a lively appreciation of the feelings and manners of all people, in whatever lands and ages﻿—a catholic sympathy, a spiritual cosmopolitanism. Not only does this exhibit itself in affectionate yearnings toward friends that are about one, and an extraordinary insight into the excellencies of their characters, but, taking a wider sweep, it can understand and feel with the heroism of philanthropists and the enthusiasm of Crusaders. The lamentation of the most ancient Thracian captive is a sincere grief to the dreamer, and the returning Camillus brought no greater joy to Rome, as he threw his defiant sword into the scale, than over the chasm of ages he sends thrilling into the hashish-eater’s heart. Whether it is the Past or the Present that is read or heard, he sorrows in all its woes and rejoices in all its rejoicings.

===Chinese===

在成千上万不断复现的情形中，为了明智地指引我们的行动航向，知晓两点之间直线最短、整体等于部分之和以及无数类似的公理事实是绝对必需的；倘若在每一个具体考量中都必须动用尺规与天平，并依据感官来进行逐一衡量，势必将极大地阻碍行动的效率。因此，这些真理在每个人面前皆显露在一种彰显其普适性与必然性的光芒之中；只需将其陈述出来，在任何地方皆能获得毫无异议的认同赞许。动物并非上帝的宏大劳作主体，而是人类的仆役；因此，它不需要直觉这般机能，它那卑微一天的劳作既不需要雷厉风行亦无须精密严整；当它被征调入人类的役使中拉车或推磨时，其主人的直觉便会通过缰绳与笼头引导它的前行。

毫无疑问，随着我们行动领域的拓宽，我们的直觉眼界亦将随之扩展；不仅因为若非如此，我们必将因自身的短视昏聩、以及感到无法与周遭环境步伐同频推进而倍感羞愧与沮丧，更因为在永恒建筑的恢宏蓝图之中，上帝绝不会让践行其旨意的工匠在行动中处处受到束缚掣肘。

在叙述中这略显散漫的部分里，我还想提及大麻在未达到谵妄高峰时所特有的另一个事实。我指的是对所有国度与时代的各色人群的情感与举止所产生的鲜活共鸣鉴赏——一种博大的同情共感，一种属灵的世界主义。这不仅体现在对身旁密友的深情眷恋、以及对其性格卓越之处的非凡洞察上，而且拓宽至更宏大的范畴，它能深刻理解并感同身受慈善家的英勇壮举与十字军东征将士的炽热豪情。最古老色雷斯（Thracian）战俘的哀鸣，在梦境漫游者心中是无比真挚的悲恸；而归来的卡米卢斯（Camillus）将他桀骜的利剑掷向天平时给罗马带来的狂喜，越过岁月的鸿沟，亦同样令食大麻者的心脏为之震撼共鸣。无论是阅读还是聆听关于“过去”还是“当下”的记载，他皆在其所有悲苦中一同哀恸，在其所有喜乐中一同欢欣。

===Original===

He understands all feelings; his mind is malleable to all thoughts; his susceptibilities run into the mould of all emotions.
Sitting in this fused state of mind, I have heard the old ladies of the Latin time, as they sat gossiping over spindle and distaff, keep up their perpetual round of “*inquit*” and “*papae*” with as distinct and as kind appreciation as were they our own beloved American aunts and grandmothers, knitting after tea amid the interchange of “says he” and “do tell.” For Epaminondas, coming glorious from Leuctra, I could have hurrahed as enthusiastically as any Thebian of them all, or hobnobbed with Horace over his

> “Pocula veteris Massici”

with a true Roman zest and full-heartedness. At such times no anachronism seems surprising; time is treated as an insignificant barrier to those souls who, in the element of their generous humanity, possess the only true bond of conjunction, and a bond which, though now so elastic that it permits years and leagues to keep souls apart, shall one day pull with a force strong enough to bring all congenialities together, in place as well as in state, and every man shall be with those whom, for their inner qualities, he has most deeply lived through all his life.

===Chinese===

他通晓一切情感；他的心智可塑于一切思想；他的敏锐感官能契合于一切情绪的模具之中。

端坐在这般神思交融的心智状态中，我听闻拉丁时代的老妇人们围坐在纺锤与线轴旁闲话家常，持续不断地重复着她们那一套“他说（*inquit*）”与“哎呀（*papae*）”，其清晰与亲切的理解体会，宛若她们正是我们自己深爱的美利坚姑母与祖母、在茶后一边编织一边互道“他说呀”与“可不是嘛”一般。对于在留克特拉（Leuctra）战役中凯旋归来、满载荣耀的埃帕米农达（Epaminondas），我能如任何忒拜人一样热烈欢呼雀跃；抑或怀着真正的罗马豪情与满腔热忱，同贺拉斯（Horace）共饮他的

> “古老马西克美酒之杯（*Pocula veteris Massici*）”。

在那些时刻，任何时代错位皆不足为奇；时间对于那些在慷慨人性的要素中拥有唯一真正纽带的心灵而言，不过是微不足道的屏障——这一纽带如今虽弹性十足，任由岁月与山川将灵魂阻隔两地，终有一日必将以无比强劲的力量将所有意气相投的心灵紧紧拉拢在一起，无论在空间上还是在状态中，使每个人皆能同那些他一生中因内在品质而最深切共鸣的至亲相伴相守。

## XIV Hail! Pythagoras / 第十四章 万岁！毕达哥拉斯
===Original===

The hemisphere of sky which walls us in is something more than a mere product of the laws of sight. It is our shield from unbearable visions. Within our little domain of view, girt by the horizon and arched by the dome of heaven, there is enough of sorrow, enough of danger, yes, enough of beauty and of mirth visible to occupy the soul abundantly in any one single beholding. That lesser and unseen hemisphere which bounds our hearing is also amply large, for within it echo enough of music and lamentation to fill all susceptibility to the utmost. In this world we are but half spirit; we are thus able to hold only the perceptions and emotions of half an orb. Once fully rounded into symmetry ourselves, we shall have strength to bear the pressure of influences from a whole sphere of truth and loveliness.
It is this present half-developed state of ours which makes the infinitude of the hashish awakening so unendurable, even when its sublimity is the sublimity of delight. We have no longer anything to do with horizons, and the boundary which was at once our barrier and our fortress is removed, until we almost perish from the inflow of perceptions.
One most powerful realization of this fact occurred to me when hashish had already become a fascination and a habit. In the broad daylight of a summer afternoon I was walking in the full possession of delirium. For an hour the expansion of all visible things had been growing toward its height; it now reached it, and to the fullest extent I apprehended what is meant by the infinity of space. Vistas no longer converged; sight met no barrier; the world was horizonless, for earth and sky stretched endlessly onward in parallel planes. Above me the heavens were terrible with the glory of a fathomless depth. I looked up, but my eyes, unopposed, every moment penetrated farther and farther into the immensity, and I turned them downward, lest they should presently intrude into the fatal splendors of the Great Presence. Unable to bear visible objects, I shut my eyes. In one moment a colossal music filled the whole hemisphere above me, and I thrilled upward through its environment on visionless wings.

===Chinese===

将我们围合在内的苍穹半球，绝非仅仅是视觉法则的产物。它是守护我们免受难以承受之异象侵袭的坚固盾牌。在我们由地平线合围、由天穹拱顶笼罩的狭小视野之内，已然存在着足够多的悲伤、足够多的危险，是的，亦有足够多的美与欢乐在目，足以让灵魂在任何单次凝视中充盈满溢。那限定我们听觉的较小且不可见的半球亦同样辽阔无垠，因为在其中回荡着足够多的仙乐与哀歌，足以将一切感官敏锐度填塞至极致。在此世间，我们不过拥有半副灵性；因此我们仅能承受半个天球的感知与情绪。唯有一旦我们自身的对称性得以完满塑造，我们方有力量承受来自整个由真理与可爱构成的浑圆天球之力量的重压。

正是我们当下这半发育的状态，使得大麻觉醒的无垠浩瀚变得如此令人难以承受，即便其崇高乃是至福的崇高。我们再也与地平线毫无瓜葛，那道曾经既是我们的屏障又是我们要塞的界线已被彻底抹去，直至我们因感知的大量涌入而几乎濒临消亡。

当大麻已然演变成为一种难以抗拒的诱惑与习惯之时，我曾对这一事实产生过一次最为震撼的领悟。在一个夏日午后的灿烂天光之下，我在彻底陷入谵妄的状态中漫步前行。整整一个钟头里，所有可见之物的膨胀一直在朝着巅峰蔓延；此时它终于登峰造极，我最彻底地领悟到了“空间的无垠”究竟所指为何。远景不再收敛于焦点；视线未遇任何阻隔；世界失去了地平线，因为大地与天空在平行的平面上无休止地向前延展。在我上方，诸天因深不可测的幽深荣光而显得令人战栗。我仰望苍穹，但双眼在毫无遮拦之下，每一刻都愈发深邃地洞穿至浩渺苍茫之中，我赶忙将视线转向下方，生怕目光会在顷刻间冒犯侵入“至高临在”那毁灭性的威光之中。因无法承受目之所及的物体，我阖上了双眸。一刹那间，一阵巍峨宏大的仙乐充盈了我上方的整个半球，我在它所构筑的环境中，振动着无形的双翼向着高空战栗飞升。

===Original===

It was not song, it was not instruments, but the inexpressible spirit of sublime sound﻿—like nothing I had ever heard﻿—impossible to be symbolized; intense, yet not loud; the ideal of harmony, yet distinguishable into a multiplicity of exquisite parts.
I opened my eyes, but it still continued. I sought around me to detect some natural sound which might be exaggerated into such a semblance; but no, it was of unearthly generation, and it thrilled through the universe an inexplicable, a beautiful, yet an awful symphony.
Suddenly my mind grew solemn with the consciousness of a quickened perception. And what a solemnity is that which the hashish-eater feels at such a moment! The very beating of his heart is silenced; he stands with his finger on his lip; his eye is fixed, and he becomes a very statue of awful veneration. The face of such a man, however little glorified in feature or expression during his ordinary states of mind, I have stood and looked upon with the consciousness that I was beholding more of the embodiment of the truly sublime than any created thing could ever offer me.
I looked abroad on fields, and waters, and sky, and read in them a most startling meaning. I wondered how I had ever regarded them in the light of dead matter, at the farthest only *suggesting* lessons. They were now, as in my former vision, grand symbols of the sublimest spiritual truths﻿—truths never before even feebly grasped, and utterly unsuspected.
Like a map, the arcana of the universe lay bare before me. I saw how every created thing not only typifies, but springs forth from some mighty spiritual law as its offspring, its necessary external development﻿—not the mere clothing of the essence, but the essence incarnate.
I am aware that, in this recital, I may seem to be repeating what I have said before of my dreadful night of insight; but between the two visions there was this difference, the view did not stop here. While that music was pouring through the great heavens above me, I became conscious of a numerical order which ran through it, and in marking this order, I beheld it transferred to every movement of the universe. Every sphere wheeled on in its orbit, every emotion of the soul arose and fell, every smallest moss and fungus germinated and grew according to some peculiarity of numbers which severally governed them, and was most admirably typified by them in return.

===Chinese===

那并非歌声，亦非乐器之音，而是崇高音律无可名状的纯粹灵性——不同于我所听闻过的任何声响——根本无法用凡尘具象来象征；激昂热烈，却并不喧嚣刺耳；乃是和谐的至高典范，却又能分辨出无数精妙绝伦的细微声部。

我睁开双眼，但那仙乐仍在持续。我环顾周遭，试图探寻是否有某种自然声响可能被夸张渲染成了这般模样；然而绝非如此，它乃是超凡脱俗之造化，化作一首无法索解、瑰丽绝伦却又令人敬畏的交响乐，在整个宇宙间回荡悸动。

突然间，我的心智因感知被陡然唤醒的自觉而变得庄严无比。食大麻者在此类时刻所感受到的，是何等神圣肃穆的庄严啊！连他自己的心跳声都归于沉寂；他伫立着，以指按唇；他的双目凝滞不动，化作了一尊令人肃然起敬的虔敬雕像。这样一个人的面庞，无论在寻常的心智状态下其五官或神态是多么缺乏光彩，当我伫立凝视着他时，心中皆深知自己所目睹的，乃是比世间任何受造物所能展现更为真实的崇高化身。

我纵目远眺田野、水流与苍穹，并在其中读出了一种最令人惊心动魄的深意。我不禁纳罕，自己以往怎会仅将其视作死寂的物质、顶多只能*启发*些许教益呢？如今它们正如我先前的异象中所显现的那样，乃是最崇高的属灵真理之宏伟象征——那些此前甚至从未被微弱领会、且全然未被怀疑过的真理。

宛若一幅地图，宇宙的深奥秘奥毫无保留地展现在我眼前。我看清了每一件受造之物不仅象征着、而且是从某条伟大的属灵法则中作为其子嗣而萌发喷薄而出，成为其必然的外在演进形态——并非仅仅是本质的外衣，而是本质本身的真切化身。

我深知，在这番叙述中，我似乎在重复自己此前关于那个充斥着洞察力的可怖之夜所言之语；但在这两次异象之间却存在着这般差异：眼前的景象并未止步于此。当那阵仙乐在我头顶浩瀚的诸天中奔涌之时，我觉察到有一套数理秩序正贯穿其中；而在留意到这套秩序的瞬间，我目睹它被投射到了宇宙的每一次运转之中。每一个天球在各自的轨道上旋转运转，灵魂的每一次情绪起伏翻腾，每一株最微小的苔藓与真菌皆依据支配它们各自的某种特殊数律而萌发滋长，并反过来将其最为精妙地象征具象。

===Original===

An exquisite harmony of proportion reigned through space, and I seemed to realize that the music which I heard was but this numerical harmony making itself objective through the development of a grand harmony of tones.
The vividness with which this conception revealed itself to me made it a thing terrible to bear alone. An unutterable ecstasy was carrying me away, but I dared not abandon myself to it. I was no seer who could look on the unveiling of such glories face to face.
An irrepressible yearning came over me to impart what I beheld, to share with another soul the weight of this colossal revelation. With this purpose I scrutinized this vision; I sought in it for some characteristic which might make it translatable to another mind. There was none. In absolute incommunicableness it stood apart. For it, in spoken language, there was no symbol.
For a long time﻿—how long a hashish-eater alone can know﻿—I was in an agony. I searched every pocket for my pencil and notebook, that I might at least set down some representative mark which would afterward recall to me the lineaments of my apocalypse. They were not with me. Jutting into the water of the brook along which I then wandered, and which, before and afterward, was my sole companion through so many ecstasies, lay a broad, flat stone. “Glory in the highest!” I shouted, exultingly; “I will at least grave on this tablet some hieroglyph of what I feel.” Tremblingly I sought for my knife; that, too, was gone! It was then that, in a frenzy, I threw myself prostrate on the stone, and with my nails sought to make some memorial scratch upon it. Hard, hard as flint! In despair I stood up.
Suddenly there came a sense as if some invisible presence walking the dread paths of the vision with me, yet at a distance, as if separated from my side by a long flow of time. Taking courage, I cried, “Who has ever been here before me? who, in years past, has shared with me this unutterable view?” In tones which linger with me to this day, a grand, audible voice responded “Pythagoras!” In an instant I was calm. I heard the footsteps of that sublime sage echoing upward through the ages, and in celestial light I read my vision unterrified, since it had burst upon his sight before me.
For years previous I had been perplexed with his mysterious philosophy.

===Chinese===

一种精妙绝伦的比例和谐统治着浩瀚空间，而我似乎领悟到了：我所听闻的仙乐，正是这种数理和谐通过宏大音调和谐的展现而使自身客观化。

这一构想向我显现的鲜活性，使得它成为一件令人难以独自承受的可怖之事。一阵难以言状的狂喜将我裹挟而去，但我却不敢任由自己沉沦其中。我并非能同这般荣耀的揭示面对面相觑的先知。

一种难以遏制的渴望袭上心头，促使我想要将目睹的一切传达出去，同另一颗灵魂共同分担这沉重庞大的宏大启示。怀着这一目的，我仔细审视着这场异象；我在其中寻找着某种可能使其转译给另一颗心智的特质。然而一无所获。它在绝对的不可传递性中巍然独立。对于它而言，在口语的世界里根本找不到任何符号象征。

在一段漫长的时间里——究竟有多久唯有食大麻者方能知晓——我饱受折磨。我搜遍了每一个口袋寻找铅笔和笔记本，以便能至少记下一丝代表性的印记，日后能帮我忆起这场启示的轮廓。可我什么也没带在身边。我当时正漫步徜徉在溪流畔——这条小溪在以往和后来的无数次狂喜中都是我唯一的伴侣——一块宽大平整的岩石正斜插在溪水之中。“至高荣耀归于上天！”我狂喜地高呼道，“我至少可以在这块石板上刻下些许代表我所感所悟的象形符号。”我颤抖着去摸我的小刀；可它竟然也不见了！就在那一刻，在一阵狂乱之中，我扑通一声趴倒在石头上，用手指甲拼命试图在上面抠出一些纪念性的划痕。坚硬，坚硬如燧石！在绝望之中，我站起身来。

突然间，我感到仿佛有某种无形的存在正同我一道漫步在这场异象的恐怖幽径之上，然而却保持着一段距离，宛如与我相隔着一段漫长的岁月之河。我鼓起勇气呼喊道：“在我之前究竟有谁曾来过这里？在过去的岁月里，有谁曾同我分享过这无可言说的壮阔奇景？”伴随着至今仍回荡在我心中的音调，一个宏大清晰的话音回应道：“毕达哥拉斯（Pythagoras）！”刹那间，我恢复了平静。我听闻那位崇高圣哲的足音跨越千古岁月激荡回响；在神圣的天光之中，我毫无惧色地审读着我的异象，因为早在我的目光之前，它便已在毕达哥拉斯眼前骤然绽放。

在此前的漫长岁月里，我一直为他那神秘莫测的哲学所困惑不解。

===Original===

I saw in him an isolation from universal contemporary mind for which I could not account. When the Ionic school was at the height of its dominance, he stood forth alone the originator of a system as distinct from it as the antipodes of mind.
The doctrine of Thales was built up by the uncertain processes of an obscure logic; that of Pythagoras seemed informed by intuition. In his assertions there had always appeared to me a grave conviction of truth, a consciousness of sincerity which gave them a great weight, though I saw them through the dim refracting medium of tradition, and grasped their meaning imperfectly. It was now given to see, in their own light, the truths which he set forth. I also saw, as to this day I firmly believe, the source whence their revelation flowed.
Tell me not that from Phoenicia he received the wand at whose signal the cohorts of the spheres came trooping up before him in review, unveiling the eternal law and itinerary of their revolutions, and pouring on his spiritual ear that tremendous music to which they marched through space. No. During half a lifetime spent in Egypt and India, both motherlands of this nepenthe, doubt not that he quaffed its apocalyptic draught, and awoke, through its terrific quickening, into the consciousness of that ever-present and all-pervading harmony “which we hear not, because the coarseness of the daily life hath dulled our ear.” The dim penetralia of the Theban Memnonium, or the silent spice-groves of the upper Indus, may have been the gymnasium of his wrestling with the mighty revealer; a priest or a gymnosophist may have been the first to anoint him with the palaestric oil, but he conquered alone. On the strange intuitive characteristics of his system; on the spherical music; on the government of all created things, and their development according to the laws of numbers; yes, on the very use of symbols, which could alone have force to the esoteric disciple (and a terrible significancy, indeed, has the simplest form to a mind hashish-quickened to read its meaning)﻿—on all of these is the legible stamp of the hashish inspiration.

===Chinese===

我在他身上看到了一种与同时代普世心智格格不入的遗世独立，对此我曾百思不得其解。当爱奥尼亚学派（Ionic school）的统治如日中天之时，他孑然独立，创立了一套与其截然相反、宛若心智两极的完整体系。

泰勒斯（Thales）的学说建立在晦涩逻辑那不确定的推演过程之上；而毕达哥拉斯的学说似乎完全源自直觉的灌注。在他的论断之中，向来展露着一种对真理的沉稳确信，一种赋予它们巨大分量的至诚自觉，尽管我是透过传统那模糊的折射介质来看待它们，且对其深意领悟得并不完全。如今，我终于得以在他所阐发的真理自身的光辉中看清它们。而且我还看清了——正如我至今坚信不疑的那样——这些启示奔涌而出的源头泉眼。

切莫告诉我他是从腓尼基（Phoenicia）获得了那根魔杖，在其挥舞之下，群星天球的军旅纷至沓来接受检阅，揭示其永恒的法则与运转轨迹，并将它们穿行宇宙时的浩瀚天籁倾注在他属灵的耳畔。绝非如此。在他耗费半生游历的埃及与印度——这两处皆是这种忘忧药草的故土家园——毋庸置疑，他曾痛饮过其启示之琼浆，并通过其惊心动魄的唤醒，觉察到了那无处不在、弥漫普世的宏大和谐——“我们之所以听不见，皆因日常生活的凡俗粗鄙磨钝了我们的双耳”。底比斯门农神庙（Theban Memnonium）幽暗的至圣所，抑或印度河上游幽静的香料树林，或许正是他同那位伟大的启示者博弈角力的练武场；某位祭司或裸形贤者（gymnosophist）或许是第一位用角力油涂抹他身躯之人，但他却是独自一人赢得了胜利。无论是在他学说体系奇异的直觉特质上、天球的交响神乐上、万物的统御及其依据数理法则的演进演化上；是的，甚至在唯有对秘传门徒方有约束力的象征符号的运用上（对于经大麻唤醒、能洞悉其深意的心智而言，最简单的几何形式亦具有令人胆寒的深远意义）——在所有这一切之上，皆清晰铭刻着源自大麻灵感的醒目烙印。

===Original===

It would be no hard task to prove, to a strong probability, at least, that the initiation to the Pythagorean mysteries, and the progressive instruction that succeeded it, to a considerable extent consisted in the employment, judiciously, if we may use the word, of hashish, as giving a critical and analytic power to the mind, which enabled the neophyte to roll up the murk and mist from beclouded truths till they stood distinctly seen in the splendor of their own harmonious beauty as an intuition.
One thing related of Pythagoras and his friends has seemed very striking to me. There is a legend that, as he was passing over a river, its waters called up to him, in the presence of his followers, “Hail! Pythagoras.” Frequently, while in the power of the hashish delirium, have I heard inanimate things sonorous with such voices. On every side they have saluted me, from rocks, and trees, and waters, and sky; in my happiness filling me with intense exultation as I heard them welcoming their master; in my agony heaping nameless curses on my head as I went away into an eternal exile from all sympathy. Of this tradition of Iamblichus I feel an appreciation which almost convinces me that the voice of the river was indeed heard, though only by the quickened mind of some hashish-glorified esoterie. Again, it may be that the doctrine of the metempsychosis was first communicated to Pythagoras by Theban priests; but the astonishing illustration which hashish would contribute to this tenet should not be overlooked in our attempt to assign its first suggestion and succeeding spread to their proper causes.
A modern critic, in defending the hypothesis that Pythagoras was an impostor, has triumphantly asked, “Why did he assume the character of Apollo at the Olympic games? Why did he boast that his soul had lived in former bodies, and that he had first been Æthalides, the son of Mercury, then Euphorbus, then Pyrrhus of Delos, and at last Pythagoras, but that he might more easily impose upon the credulity of an ignorant and superstitious people?” To us these facts seem rather an evidence of his sincerity. Had he made these assertions without proof, it is difficult to see how they would not have had a precisely contrary effect from that of paving the way to a more complete imposition upon popular credulity.

===Chinese===

要证明对毕达哥拉斯门徒（the Pythagorean）奥秘的启蒙、以及随后的循序渐进传授，在相当程度上至少极大概率包含了对大麻妥善审慎的运用（倘若我们可以用这个词的话），绝非难事——因为大麻赋予了心智批判与分析的能力，使得新入门者能够驱散笼罩在蒙昧真理之上的重重阴霾与迷雾，直至它们在其自身和谐之美的璀璨辉煌中，作为一种直觉被清晰洞察。

关于毕达哥拉斯及其门徒流传的一则逸事，在我看来显得极为惊人。相传当他正跨越一条河流时，河水在众门徒面前向上呼喊道：“万岁！毕达哥拉斯。”在大麻谵妄的支配之下，我经常听闻无生命之物发出这般响亮的声音。它们从四面八方——从岩石、树木、水流与苍穹——向我致敬致意；在我的幸福之中，当我听闻它们恭迎自己的主宰时，心中充盈着强烈的狂喜亢奋；而在我的痛苦之中，随着我步入与一切同情彻底隔绝的永恒流放，它们又将无可名状的咒骂倾泻在我的头顶。对于扬布利柯（Iamblichus）所记载的这段传说，我深有体悟，以至于近乎坚信河水之声确曾被听闻，尽管唯有通过某位受大麻荣光加被的秘传门徒那敏锐的心智方能捕捉。此外，灵魂转世说（metempsychosis）或许最初是由底比斯的祭司传授给毕达哥拉斯的；但大麻对这一信条所能贡献的惊人阐释佐证，在我们试图为其最初的启发与随后的传播探寻确切缘由时，绝不应被等闲视之。

一位现代批评家在为“毕达哥拉斯乃是招摇撞骗之徒”的假说辩护时，曾洋洋自得地质问道：“他为何要在奥林匹克运动会上冒充阿波罗（Apollo）？他为何要吹嘘自己的灵魂曾寄居于前世的肉身之中，先是墨丘利（Mercury）之子埃塔利德斯（Æthalides），随后是欧福耳波斯（Euphorbus），继而是提洛岛的皮洛士（Pyrrhus of Delos），最后才是毕达哥拉斯——难道这不正是为了更轻易地愚弄蒙蔽愚昧迷信的民众吗？”而在我们看来，这些事实恰恰是他赤诚由衷的明证。倘若他在毫无凭据的情况下做出这些断言，很难想象它们何以不会产生恰恰相反的后果，而不是为彻底欺骗大众的轻信扫平道路。

===Original===

Upon our hypothesis it may be easily shown, not only how he could fully have believed these assertions himself, but also have given them a deep significance to the minds of his disciples.
Let us see. We will consider, for example, his assumption of the character of Phoebus at the Olympic games. Let us suppose that Pythagoras, animated with a desire of alluring to the study of his philosophy a choice and enthusiastic number out of that host who, along all the radii of the civilized world, had come up to the solemn festival at Elis, had, by the talisman of hashish, called to his aid the magic of a preternatural eloquence; that while he addressed the throng, whom he had chained into breathless attention by the weird brilliancy of his eye, the unearthly imagery of his style, and the oracular insight of his thought, the grand impression flashed upon him from the very honor he was receiving, that he was the incarnation of some sublime deity. What wonder that he burst into the acknowledgment of his godship as a secret too majestic to be hoarded up; what wonder that this sudden revelation of himself, darting forth in burning words and amid such colossal surroundings, went down with the accessories of time and place along the stream of perpetual tradition?
If I may illustrate great things by small, I well remember many hallucinations of my own which would be exactly parallel to such a fancy in the mind of Pythagoras. There is no impression more deeply stamped upon my past life than one of a walk along the brook which had so often witnessed my wrestlings with the hashish-afreet, and which now beheld me, the immortal Zeus, descended among men to grant them the sublime benediction of renovated life. For this cause I had abandoned the serene seats of Olympus, the convocation of the gods, and the glory of an immortal kingship, while by my side Hermes trod the earth with radiant feet, the companion and dispenser of the beneficence of Deity. Across lakes and seas, from continent to continent we strode; the snows of Haemus and the Himalayas crunched beneath our sandals; our foreheads were bathed with the upper light, our breasts glowed with the exultant inspiration of the golden ether.

===Chinese===

依据我们的假说，不仅能轻易阐明他何以自己能对这些断言深信不疑，而且亦能解释他何以能赋予其门徒的心智以深刻的意蕴。

让我们拭目以待。举例而言，我们来审视他在奥林匹克运动会上以福玻斯·阿波罗（Phoebus）形象自居的情形。让我们设想：毕达哥拉斯一心渴望从沿着文明世界四方轨迹汇聚到伊利斯（Elis）参加隆重盛典的人潮中，吸引一批出类拔萃且满腔热忱的菁英来研修他的哲学，借由大麻这枚护身法宝，他召唤了超自然雄辩的魔力前来相助；当他对人群发表演说时，以其眼神中诡谲的光芒、文风中超凡脱俗的意象、以及思想中神谕般的洞察力，令听众屏息凝神听得如痴如醉，从他所领受的无上敬重之中，他脑海中猛然闪过一道庄严的顿悟：他正是某位崇高神明的真身化身。他将自己的神性作为一项过于宏大而不可秘而不宣的玄机脱口承认，这又何足为奇；而在灼热的言辞之中、在如此恢宏的环境之下迸发而出的这一突如其来的自我启示，连同彼时彼地的种种附属细节，顺着永恒传统的长河代代相传流芳百世，又何足为怪呢？

倘若容我以小喻大，我清楚记得自己有许多幻觉，恰好同毕达哥拉斯心中的这种幻象完全平行吻合。在我过去的岁月里，没有什么印象比顺着那条曾屡次见证我同大麻恶魔搏斗的小溪漫步更为深刻的了——那条小溪此刻目睹了我，化身为永生不灭的宙斯（Zeus），降临人间赋予凡人以焕发生机的崇高祝福。为此，我抛弃了奥林匹斯山宁静的席位、诸神的盛大集会以及不朽王权的荣耀，而在我身旁，赫尔墨斯（Hermes）用璀璨的双足丈量着大地，作为神明恩泽的伴侣与施布者同行。越过湖泊与海洋，我们跨过一个又一个大洲；赫穆斯山（Haemus）与喜马拉雅山的积雪在我们脚下的草鞋下咯吱作响；我们的额头沐浴在九天之上的神光中，我们的胸膛因金色以太那令人狂喜的灵感灌注而炽烈燃烧。

===Original===

Now resting on Chimborazo, I poured forth a majestic blessing upon all my creatures, and in an instant, with one omniscient glance, I beheld every human dwelling-place on the whole sphere irradiated with an unspeakable joy.
I saw the king rule more wisely; the laborer return from his toil to a happier home; the park grow green with an intenser culture; the harvest-field groan under the sheaves of a more prudent and prosperous husbandry. Adown blue slopes came new and more populous flocks, led by unvexed and gladsome shepherds; a thousand healthy vineyards sprang up above their new-raised sunny terraces; every smallest heart glowed with an added thrill of exultation, and the universal rebound of joy came pouring up into my own spirit with an intensity which lit my deity with rapture.
And this was but a lay hashish-eater, mysteriously clothed in no Pallas-woven, philosophic stole, who, with his friend, walked out into the fields to enjoy his delirium among the beauties of a clear summer afternoon. What, then, of Pythagoras?
It was during this walk that one of the strangest phenomena of sight which I have ever noticed appeared to me. Every sunbeam was refracted into its primitive rays; wherever upon the landscape a pencil of light fell, between rocks or trees, it seemed a prismatic pathway between earth and heaven. The atmosphere was one network of variegated solar threads, tremulous with radiance, and distilling rapture from its fibres into all my veins.
It is singular in how many ways, during the hashish life, the harmony of creation was typified to me. The harp of the universe, which I have already mentioned, was itself once repeated invision; other representations, on a scale perhaps even as grand, have left but a dim outline upon my memory; yet there is one which, though at least thrice repeated, lost no glory by growing familiar, but more and more deepened its first impress of awe and rapture. The first time that it occurred to me was when, at the close of my walk amid the majesty of apotheosis, I sat quietly at the window of my room looking out upon the sunset which bathed the gigantic landscape before me. As yet the magnifying effect of the drug had not begun to decrease, and I gazed with fascinated eyes upon mountains which scaled heaven, and a river which was oceanic, in a breathless exultancy which vibrated on the diamond edge of pain.

===Chinese===

此刻驻足在钦博拉索山（Chimborazo）之巅，我向我的万千造化倾泻下宏大的祝福，刹那间，凭借全知的一瞥，我目睹了整个天球上的每一处人类居所皆被一种无可名状的欢愉所普照。

我看见君王统治得更为明智；劳工从辛劳中归来步入更为温馨幸福的家园；庄园在更为精心的打理下绿意盎然；丰收的田野在更为审慎富庶的耕作所结出的沉甸麦穗下欢唱呻吟。顺着苍翠的斜坡，走来了一群群崭新且更为繁盛的羊群，由毫无忧患且怡然自得的牧羊人引领前行；千座生机盎然的葡萄园自其新筑的向阳梯田上拔地而起；每一颗最微小的心脏皆因增添的一丝狂喜战栗而炽热搏动，普世欢欣的共振回响以一种令我神性因陶醉而炽烈燃烧的强度，汹涌奔腾地涌入我自己的心灵深处。

而这仅仅是一位世俗的食大麻者——身上并未披戴帕拉斯（Pallas，雅典娜）织就的哲人长袍——同他的朋友漫步走入田野，在晴朗夏日午后的绮丽风光中享用着他的谵妄。那么，毕达哥拉斯又当如何呢？

正是在这趟漫步之中，我所曾注意到的最奇异的视觉现象之一呈现在我眼前。每一缕阳光皆被折射分解为其最本原的光线；无论在一道光束落在岩石间还是林木间的风景各处，皆宛若在大地与苍穹之间架起了一道彩虹般的棱镜幽径。整片大气层化作了一张由色彩斑斓的太阳丝线编织而成的巨网，伴随着光辉剧烈颤动，并从其纤维中将狂喜的甘露滴沥注入我周身的每一根血管。

在整个大麻生涯中，造化的和谐通过如此丰富多样的方式向我具象呈现，委实奇妙无比。我先前已提及过的宇宙竖琴，本身就在异象中重现过一次；其他规模或许同样宏大的表现形式，只在我的记忆中留下了模糊的轮廓；然而唯独有一种意象，尽管至少重现过三次，却丝毫未因变得熟悉而褪色，反倒一次又一次加深了它最初带来的敬畏与狂喜震撼。它初次浮现是在我结束了那场神化威严的漫步、静静坐在房间窗前凝视着夕阳将眼前宏伟的风光尽数浸染之时。当时药物的放大效应尚未开始减退，我以心驰神往的双眸凝视着直插云霄的崇山峻岭、以及辽阔如大洋般的江河，在一种于痛苦的钻石刀刃上剧烈震颤的屏息狂喜之中久久伫立。

===Original===

Suddenly the landscape floated out of sight, and in its place there sat on the trembling ether a tremendous ship, which within itself included every portion of created being. Not a God-born essence, not a microscopic atom, but was builded up into some bulwark, beam, or spar of the colossal vessel. Its marked outline was traced with the more glorious things of creation, the baser formed its inner and hidden parts. Its sides, its stern, its bow were wrought of mighty stars whose rays interlaced; its masts were similar constellations, that at their heads, a million leagues above me, yet still distinctly radiant, bore systems of suns for lanterns. Like lanterns flashed far off upon the prow, and dazzling clouds and nebulae, filled out with the breath of an omnipotent will, strained the crystal yards upon which they hung.
Now I was transferred to the deck of this infinite ship; her name was whispered in my ear, “The Ship of the Universe,” and the helm was put into my hand. With unutterable symphonies we floated out upon the boundless space, and on the distant bows there broke in music the waves of resplendent ether. It was at this post of pilotage, steering out into the unknown void, that I felt human nature within me grow godlike to an insane excess. The helmsman, the master of all being but the Divine, I burst into a chant of triumph, which shook the starry lights above me till their clusters rained glory like wine.
I bethought me, forgetful of the infinity of the ocean we were traveling, that I might mark the rate of our progress, and so drew out my watch. Its second hand had stopped. I held it to my ear and heard it tick. Again I looked at it; the hand was motionless. Continuing my gaze, I saw it at length move slowly through one of the second-spaces, when it stopped once more. Still I looked, and at last became aware that, by the hashish expansion of time, I was enabled to realize as a quite prolonged and definite period﻿—a period as great as in our ordinary state a whole minute, at least, would appear﻿—that almost infinitesimal instant during which a second hand actually is motionless at the end of its vibration between two consecutive ticks.

===Chinese===

突然间，眼前的风光飘然而散，取而代之的是在剧烈颤动的以太之上，赫然停泊着一艘巍峨无际的巨舰，其自身包罗了所有受造之物的每一处细部。世间没有哪一种神圣天生的本质、亦无哪一个微观至极的原子，未曾被构建进这艘庞大巨舰的某处舷墙、横梁或桁桅之中。其清晰分明的轮廓乃是由造化中最为荣耀璀璨之物勾勒而成，而较为卑微之物则构成了其内部隐秘幽深的部分。它的船舷、船尾与船艏皆由光芒交织辉映的浩瀚繁星锻造而成；其桅杆亦是同样的宏伟星座，在高达我头顶百万里（league）的高耸顶端，依然清晰夺目地放射出璀璨光芒，悬挂着一个个由太阳系构成的导航提灯。犹如航灯般的炽烈光芒在船艏远方闪烁，而由全能意志的气息所吹鼓灌满的眩目云海与星云，将它们悬挂于其上的水晶帆桁绷得紧紧的。

此刻，我被移置到了这艘无垠巨舰的甲板之上；一个声音在我耳畔轻声低语道出她的名号：“宇宙之舟（The Ship of the Universe）”，而舵轮随即被塞入了我的手中。伴随着无可言表的交响神乐，我们在浩瀚无垠的虚空中悠然漂航，在远方的船艏前，璀璨以太的波涛在天籁般的旋律中碎裂成浪花。正是在这引航破浪的舵手之位上，向着未知的虚空操舵航行之际，我感到自己体内的人性以一种疯狂过度的态势滋长出了神性。作为舵手，除至高神明之外所有受造万物的主宰，我爆发出一曲凯旋的赞歌，这赞歌震颤了上方星光璀璨的群灯，直至它们如同一串串成熟的葡萄般倾泻下宛如醇酒的万道荣光。

忘却了我们所航行的海洋的无垠无限，我心想或许可以测定一下我们前行的速率，于是掏出了怀表。秒针已经停摆了。我将它贴在耳边，听见它在规律地嘀嗒作响。我又低头看它；指针依然纹丝不动。我继续目不转睛地注视着它，终于看见它顺着某一格秒针刻度缓慢移动了一格，随后便再次停顿了下来。我依然紧盯着不放，终于恍然大悟：由于大麻对时间的极度膨胀放大，我得以将两声连续的嘀嗒声之间、秒针在振动末端实际上纹丝不动的近乎无限微小的瞬间，真真切切地感知为一段相当漫长而确凿的时期——这段时期是如此漫长，以至于在我们寻常的状态下，至少显得宛若整整一分钟那般悠长。

## XV “Then Seeva Opened on the Accursed One His Eye of Anger” / 第十五章 “随后湿婆（Seeva）对那遭诅咒者睁开了愤怒之眼”
===Original===

In the agonies of hashish, which now became more and more frequent, a new element began to develop itself toward a terrible symmetry, which afterward made it effective for the direst spiritual evil. This was the appearance of Deity upon the stage of my visionary life, now sublimely grand in very person, and now through the intermediation of some messenger or sign, yet always menacing, wrathful, or avenging, in whatever form or manner the visitation might be made. The myriad voices which, earlier in my enchanted life, I had heard from Nature through all her mysterious passages of communication, now died down forever, or, rather, became absorbed into one colossal and central voice, which spoke with the force of a fiat, and silenced my own faint replies like the sentence of inevitable doom.
At first I was calmly warned. Repeatedly, as I sat in an elysium of rosy languor, banqueting upon all that could exalt the inner sense into the serenest ecstasy, the hand that wrote upon the wall has invaded the sanctity of my feast, and its dread tracery has made me suddenly afraid. In characters of light I have seen it written, “Beware how thou triflest with a mysterious power of the Most High!” and an audible voice, whose divinity at the moment I no more doubted than my own humanity, has added its injunction, “Beware! beware!” Anticipating nothing but an uninterrupted procession of sublime images and the choral music which had so often ravished me out of the walls of sense, I have in an instant shuddered with unutterable terror as I felt the unlooked-for finger of some awful presence marking out downward channels for my upwelling thought, and solemnly forcing its streams into them with a power which bore no doubtful tokens of irresistibleness, but commanded even my own assent to the impossibility of escape.
At length the reasons of my punishment were shown me. Here again, as audibly as man talks with man, I was told, “Thou hast lifted thyself above humanity to peer into the speechless secrets before thy time; and thou shalt be smitten﻿—smitten﻿—smitten.” As the last echo of the sentence died away, it always began its execution in Promethean pangs. At last even the faintest suggestion of the presence of Deity possessed a power to work me ill which hardly the haunting of demons had been able to produce before.

===Chinese===

在如今愈发频繁发作的大麻剧痛折磨中，一种全新的元素开始朝着某种可怖的对称演化发展，日后正是它成了施加最严酷属灵恶运的致命利器。这便是在我的异象舞台上神明的显现——时而以其真身展现出至高无上的威严，时而借由某位使者或预兆的居间传递，但无论以何种形式或方式降临，祂始终显得杀气腾腾、怒火中烧或满怀复仇之意。在早先那段如梦如幻的岁月里，我曾从大自然所有神秘的沟通渠道中听闻到的万千声音，如今已然彻底沉寂，或者更确切地说，尽数被吸纳融入了一声巍峨磅礴的至尊之音中；那声音带着神明律令的绝对权威发号施令，将我微弱的应答悉数压制，宛若降下无可逃避的末日判决。

起初，我只是受到平缓的警告。不知有多少次，当我沉浸在一片玫瑰色的慵懒极乐仙境中、尽情享用着能将内在感官超拔入最恬静狂喜之境的一切珍馐时，那只在墙壁上书写文字的神奇之手便侵入了我的神圣盛宴，其可怖的笔迹令我顿生惊恐。在由光芒凝成的文字中，我目睹其上写道：“切莫玩弄至高者的神秘伟力，当心尔之下场！”而一个清晰可闻的话音——在彼时彼刻，我对那声音的神圣性毫不怀疑，恰如对自身的人性确信不疑那般——紧接着增添了祂的训诫：“当心！当心！”原本满心期盼着目不暇接的崇高意象纷至沓来，以及曾屡屡将我超拔出感官藩篱的合唱神乐，我却在刹那间因无可言喻的恐惧而战栗不已——因为我感受到某位可怖临在者那不期而至的手指，正在为我胸中奔涌的思潮划定急转直下的沟渠，并以一种毫无抗拒可能的威严伟力，庄严地将我的思绪洪流强行逼入其中，迫使我不得不由衷承认逃脱之绝无可能。

最终，向我昭示了我遭受惩罚的缘由。在这里，正如人与人促膝交谈般清晰可辨，我被告知：“你竟敢狂妄凌驾于人性之上，妄图提前窥探不可言传的奥秘；你必遭击打——击打——重重击打。”随着判决的最后一道余音消散殆尽，它总是在普罗米修斯式的酷刑剧痛中即刻付诸执行。到最后，哪怕是神明临在的最微弱暗示，亦拥有了残害折磨我的伟力，其威势甚至是以往群魔的纠缠骚扰所未曾企及的。

===Original===

At one time I well remember beholding a colossal veiled figure part the drapery of sombre clouds which hung over the horizon, and appear upon a platform which I supposed to be the stage of the universe. No sound, no radiance issued from behind the veil, yet when the mysterious figure lifted his hands, I cried, “It is the Day of Judgment, and my doom is being pronounced!” Then I fled for my soul, and cowered in the darkest spot that I could find.
One tremendous vision occurred to me during the progress of one of these peculiar states, which, while it filled me with the agony of despair for my own fate, still gave me an inconceivable pain for another being. In the heavens I heard a voice of weeping; no plaintive wail like that of woman in affliction, no passionate cry like that of a strong man riven by distress, but some nameless agony, foreshadowed by a solemn voice of woe, which spoke of universal creation suffering fearfully at its centre, life drying up at its fountainhead of being. “Who weeps?” I cried in terror. And the answer was returned to me out of the viewless air, “The Mighty One, who was of old held supreme, hath discovered that his supremacy is void. Fate, blind Fate, that hath no ear for thy yearnings, sits mover of the spring of all things, and He to whom thou prayest is a discrowned King.” Ah! well might there be such weeping in the heavens! After all, we had no Lord, no God but Destiny. And I saw dynasties rush down in aimless ruin; good and evil met in eternal shock; there should be no prevalence to Right; the souls of all humanity were but atoms hurled onward through an infinite, lawless Chaos. In my own spirit there sounded an echo to the celestial groaning, and with tearless horror I went straying through the rayless abyss of accident, a tortured creature without a goal. “My God,” I whispered, “annihilate me!” Words of accursed folly! God no longer lived.
I threw myself upon the earth, and clutched its dead, ungoverned dust in my writhing fingers. I called no longer upon God, and was dumb because Fate was deaf. I cursed the day that I was born﻿—meaningless, still meaningless, since there was no power who could authenticate the curse. I lay balancing the chances of being blotted out. Somewhere in the eternities a crash might end me. Forever? What if my disrupted being should float together in cycles measurelessly on?

===Chinese===

有一次，我清楚记得自己望见一个巨大的蒙面身影，拨开了低垂于地平线之上的阴沉云翳幕帘，赫然出现在一座我自以为是“宇宙之台”的高台之上。在面纱背后未曾发出任何声息，亦无半点光辉，然而当那神秘的身影缓缓抬起双手时，我失声痛呼：“这是最后的审判之日，我的末日判决正在被宣读！”随后我为了保全灵魂狼狈逃窜，瑟缩在所能寻觅到的最阴暗的角落里。

在这类特殊境遇的发展过程中，我曾经历过一场震撼心魄的宏大异象——它在因我自身的命运而让我陷入绝望剧痛的同时，亦让我为另一个至高存在体会到了一种无可想象的深切悲怆。在诸天之上，我听见了一阵啜泣之声；那绝非妇人身处苦难中的幽怨哀啼，亦非伟岸男子遭逢逆境被撕碎时的激愤呼号，而是一种由庄严悲凄的哀音所预示的无可名状的剧痛折磨——那声音诉说着普世造化在其核心正遭受着可怕的摧残，生命之泉正在其存在的源头泉眼处逐渐干涸。“是谁在哭泣？”我在恐惧中惊呼。从无形的虚空之中向我传来了回应：“那位自古被奉为至高无上的‘全能者’，方才发现自己的至尊王权实乃虚妄。命运，那对你的渴望充耳不闻的盲目命运，正端坐于万物发条的操盘之位，而你所祈求的那位，不过是一位被剥夺了王冠的废王。”啊！诸天之上确该有此等痛哭啊！归根结底，我们根本没有主宰，除却“定数命运”之外别无神明。我目睹诸多王朝在漫无目的的废墟中轰然崩塌；善与恶陷入了永恒的撞击冲突；正义将不复存在；整个人类的灵魂不过是在一片无垠且毫无规律的浑沌深渊中向前乱撞的渺小原子。在我自己的灵魂深处，激荡起了对天界呻吟的共鸣回响，我带着欲哭无泪的骇然，在一处充满意外巧合的暗无天日深渊中游荡徘徊，成为一个失去目标的受难生灵。“我的上帝，”我低语道，“让我彻底湮灭吧！”这真是一句该死的愚妄之语！上帝已然不复存活。

我将自己重重摔在大地之上，抽搐扭曲的手指死死抓住了毫无管辖的死寂尘土。我不再向上帝呼求，我陷入了死寂的沉默，因为命运本身乃是一介聋子。我诅咒自己降生的那一天——毫无意义，依然毫无意义，因为世间根本没有任何力量能够印证这道诅咒。我躺在地上权衡着彻底被抹杀的可能性。在永恒岁月的某处，一场轰然巨响或许能终结我的存在。是永远终结吗？倘若我支离破碎的存在，又在漫无边际的未来轮回周期中重新汇聚漂浮在一起，那又该当如何？

===Original===

Reunited, I should wander once more a godless wretch!
From horizon to horizon there flashed a quick glory; heaven rang through all its dome with a multitude of tremendous bands, and a sound of chanting joined in the symphony. “Ah! what is this?” I said, and started up. “I hear a harmony, and Fate knows only discords.” Again the aerial voice responded, but now in a triumphant song, “After all, there is a Supreme; he rules whose right it is; there is no destiny but God, and he is over all forever.” I leaped into the air﻿—I shouted for joy. The hope of the ages was sure﻿—there was a God!
Yet few of my visions of the Divine, as bitterly I tested in many a trial of fire, were to have an issue so blessed as this.
Through the watches of a long and lonely night I had been sitting, with no other companion than my crusted lamp, and the shapes of strange men and things passed by me ceaselessly in tides of pain and pleasure. At length I found myself in the highest story of an unknown and desolate house, surrounded by blank walls, and lighted by a single narrow window. “This room,” spoke the hashish voice, “is that which thou callest Time. Outside the whirl the resistless, the unbounded winds of Eternity.”
I went to the window, and, looking out, saw nothing; but the heavy roar of a storm-lashed atmosphere shook the panes. A strange fascination tempted me to draw nearer to the tempest. I threw up the sash; in one moment the wind of eternity came rushing in; the foundations of my building shook, and straightway, by those stormy wings, every atom of it was winnowed out of sight, and, houseless, I found myself alone among the infinitudes. For a while I was blown hither and thither unconsciously. Then, coming to myself, I found that I had been wafted to the door of a certain friend of mine, who doubtless would care for me in my bewilderment of suffering.
It was now four o’clock of a midsummer morning, and the western hills, that I could see through a hall window, began to be impurpled by reflection from the opposite horizon of the dawn. My friend was an early riser, and he would, perhaps, be willing to walk with me, for I could not endure to sit still for a moment. “Baldwin!” I cried; “Baldwin, it is necessary that I should speak with you,” at the same time knocking stoutly until I aroused him from sleep.

===Chinese===

重聚之后，我岂不是又要沦为一个没有神明庇佑的可怜流浪汉！

从地平线的一端到另一端，猛然闪过了一道迅疾的荣光；整个苍穹天顶在无数宏大乐队的轰鸣中回荡齐鸣，伴随着赞颂咏唱之声融入了交响乐之中。“啊！这是什么？”我惊愕地站起身来，“我听见了和谐之音，而命运却向来唯知混乱与龃龉。”空中再度响起了话音，然而此刻却化作了一首凯旋的赞歌：“归根结底，至高者依然长存；执掌王权者实至名归；除上帝之外别无命运，祂永远统治万有。”我一跃而起——喜极而泣大声欢呼。万古的期盼坚不可摧——世间确有上帝！

然而，正如我在诸多烈火淬炼的严酷考验中所痛苦体验到的那样，我关于神明的异象极少能拥有这般蒙福的结局。

在漫长而孤寂的守夜时分里，我独自端坐着，唯有一盏结满油垢的油灯与我为伴，奇异的人物与形貌在痛苦与欢愉的潮汐中不断从我身旁无休止地掠过。最终，我发觉自己置身于一处荒凉未知的宅邸顶层，四周皆是空无一物的白墙，仅有一扇狭窄的窗户透进微光。“这间屋子，”大麻的嗓音说道，“便是你所称的‘时间’。窗外呼啸狂转的，乃是永恒那无可阻挡、漫无边际的狂风。”

我走向窗前，极目远眺，眼前空无一物；但被风暴鞭笞的大气所发出的沉闷轰鸣震颤着窗玻璃。一种诡异的魔力诱惑着我向暴风雨靠得更近。我一把推开窗户；刹那间，永恒的罡风呼啸席卷而入；我所居建筑的地基剧烈震颤，转瞬之间，在那狂暴的双翼扫荡之下，房屋的每一个原子皆被扬散消逝在视线之外，我沦为一个无家可归之人，孑然一身被抛弃在浩瀚的无垠深渊之中。有一阵子，我在无意识中被狂风吹得东飘西荡。随后，待我神志稍清，我发现自己已被吹送到了某位好友的门前，在这饱受痛苦折磨的恍惚之中，他无疑会悉心照料我。

此刻已是仲夏清晨的四点钟，透过门厅的窗户眺望西面的山峦，在黎明相反地平线的映照下已然开始泛起一抹紫晕。我的朋友素有早起的习惯，他或许愿意陪我出去走走，因为我此刻根本无法安静端坐哪怕一刻。“鲍德温（Baldwin）！”我呼喊道，“鲍德温，我必须同你谈谈，”一边用力重重敲门，直至将他从睡梦中唤醒。

===Original===

It was at first very difficult for me to persuade him how intensely I was suffering, for my habit of self-control subdued even my face. At last we were in the open air, and I walked clinging to Baldwin’s arm. I said little, for I had no power to speak above a whisper, and in disjointed sentences. Coming to the steps which led from my own entry, we sat down for a few moments’ rest.
