For the first time in all the tremendous stretch of my spellbound eternity heard I the voice of sympathy or saw I an exemplar of escape. Though I might never look upon his face on earth, disenthralled from the bodily I should know him immediately, for I was bound to him by ties spun from the distaff of a supernatural hand.
I returned homeward, bearing in my mind almost the exact words of that vivid and most truthful recital. So powerfully did its emotion possess me as to supplant entirely that of the drug, which did not once render itself perceptible.
There is a rich lesson of deep springs of human action taught by the old history, wherein he who in after years was to make the name of Carthage glorious among the peoples uplifted his hand of adjuration in the presence of his father.

===Chinese===

然而，当我带着守财奴般贪婪吞噬的目光，逐字逐句研读、沉思并反复重读每一行文字时，我发现它同我过去的亲身经历竟然存在着如此令人惊骇的惊人相似之处，以至于冷汗直自额头涔涔而下，我不禁脱口惊呼：“此人曾真真切切踏入过我自己的灵魂！”我们两人皆曾被苍天所唾弃抛弃；皆曾攀爬登上神明的至尊特权之巅，随后又被重重摔落深渊；皆曾在可怖诱惑的耳语驱使下拔出过罪恶的利刃；皆曾沦为遭世人背弃、厌弃与唾骂的弃儿。那么，我是否当将这种相似性推向更远？*他已然永远戒除了大麻。*这一问题是何等严酷地震颤着我的灵魂啊！刹那间，宛若一幕盛大的华美游行，魔法诱惑的无尽荣耀从我眼前奔流掠过。从往昔岁月之中“记忆”款款走来，摇曳着令人沉醉的香炉；在那缕缕升腾的芬芳烟云之上，勾勒出了一方比巫术在迫使命运臣服的烟雾中为凝视未来的痴狂者所描摹的更为崇高的诸天、更为绮丽的大地。在彼处，昔日的剧痛折磨已无立锥之地；一切皆是恬静、狂喜与天启神示。难道我当将所有这一切永远舍弃吗？

*愿上帝助我，我愿意！*

我并不知晓那篇文章的作者究竟是谁。对于他的尊姓大名我甚至未曾有过半点猜想。然而对于他，我却感到了一种共鸣同理；是的，尽管这超脱凡俗，却是一种足以驱使我履行至高感恩职责的深情厚谊。他洞穿了我此前从未被打破的深沉孤独；在彼此未曾察觉对方存在的情况下，我们曾并肩漫步在可怕阴影的幽谷之中。正如世间没有任何其他人能像他那样体谅我，他能深切体会。正如无人能向我进言开导，他能予以劝勉。在我那受魔咒禁锢的漫长永恒之中，我头一次听闻到了同情相恤的声音，抑或望见了一位逃出生天的楷模先驱。尽管在尘世间我或许永远无法一睹他的真容，但一旦脱离了肉体的羁绊，我定能瞬间将他认出，因为维系我们心灵的纽带，乃是由超自然神圣之手的纺锤所编织而成。

我踏上了归途，脑海中几乎一字不差地铭记着那篇鲜活而至诚的真实记述。其澎湃的情感如此强有力地占据了我，以至于彻底取代了药物的作用，后者甚至未能显露出丝毫的端倪。

在久远的历史之中，蕴含着一则关于人类行动深刻源泉的丰厚启示——在日后的岁月里注定要让迦太基（Carthage）之名在万民之中光耀显赫的那位英豪，曾在他父亲的面前高高举起宣誓的右手。

===Original===

From him out of whose original fount he came, and in whose depths his earliest waves of being found their noblest, their truest echo of response, most naturally did he draw that full tide of strength which through all barriers was to bear him on until he whelmed in the deluge of inherited vengeance the territory of his foe.
No Hannibal was I, but the struggling sufferer under long soldered thrall of sorcery, groaning for a deliverance which I just dared to tempt; no Hamilear wert thou, my father, for the hands with which thou supportedst mine in their final vow of liberty were wet, not with the blood of war, but the tears of a most precious compassion; and as before thee, on that last night of my bondage, I took the oath which opened up my prison-doors, from thy presence I won a sustaining force of will which, through many a day of fray and weariness, was to press me on (in all reverence to the majestic memorials of past time) against a mightier, a subtler enemy than Rome!
After thus sealing my deliverance, my next step was to discover the author of the article in Putnam’s, which had determined me to it at first. This, through the kind courtesy of some of its presiding minds, I was in a few days enabled to do. To the author I then wrote, trusting to no other introduction than that of our common ground and the sympathies of human nature. I asked counsel upon the best means of softening the pathway of my escape, for I had seen enough in my former effort to assure me that it would be a very hard one. Moreover, the simple possession of a letter from one who had been so instrumental in originally effecting my release would be a powerful aid toward rendering it permanent.
A very short time elapsed before I received an answer to my inquiries. My anxiety could not have made it more full than it was of information and assistance; my gratitude could not have exaggerated the value of its sympathy and encouragement. But for the sacredness which to a mind of any refinement invests a correspondence of such nature, I could not refrain from here giving it publicity. It strengthened my resolution, it opened for me a cheering sky of hope, it pointed me to expedients for insuring success, it mitigated the sufferings of the present. It is, and ever will be, treasured among the most precious archives of my life.

===Chinese===

从孕育他诞生的源头泉眼之中，在那他最早的生命波澜寻觅到最崇高、最真挚共鸣回应的深处，他极其自然地汲取了那股浩荡磅礴的力量洪流——那股力量将冲破一切阻碍裹挟着他向前，直至将世仇的疆土尽数淹没在世代相承的复仇洪水之中。

我绝非汉尼拔（Hannibal），而是一名在巫术长期熔铸的枷锁下痛苦挣扎的受难者，呻吟着渴盼一场我才刚刚斗胆尝试的解脱救赎；而你亦非哈米尔卡（Hamilcar），我的父亲啊，因为在迈向自由的最终誓言中支撑着我双手的你的双手，浸润的并非战争的鲜血，而是最珍贵的慈怜泪水；在那场囚徒生涯的最后一夜，当我伫立在你面前许下开启我牢狱大门的誓言之时，我从你的临在中赢得了坚不可摧的意志支撑力——这股力量在日后无数充满征战与疲惫的漫长岁月里，将督促着我（怀着对过往岁月壮丽遗存的一切虔敬）向着一个比罗马更为强大、更为诡谲狡诈的敌人挺进拼搏！

在以此方式坚定了我的解脱决心之后，我的下一步便是探寻出最初促成我下定决心的《普特南月刊》上那篇文章的作者。借由该刊几位主事菁英的深情厚意，数日之内我便如愿以偿。随后我致信那位作者，仅凭借我们共同的经历底色与人性的同情共理作为引荐。我向他请教能够平抑铺平我逃亡坦途的最佳良策，因为在先前的尝试中我已经历得足够多，深知这将是一条极其险阻的苦途。更有甚者，仅仅是拥有一封出自最初有力促成我获释之人的亲笔信函，亦将成为令这一解脱持久永固的强大助力。

没过多久，我便收到了对我求教的回复。纵使以我最焦灼的期盼，那封信所包含的信息与襄助亦绝不可能更为详尽充实；纵使以我最深沉的感激，亦无法夸大其同情与鼓舞之无上价值。若非出于任何有修养的心灵赋予此类通信的神圣私密性，我定忍不住要将其在此公之于众。它坚定了我的决绝意志，它为我开启了一片令人振奋的希望晴空，它向我指明了确保成功的权宜良方，它抚平了当下的种种痛苦。它如今是、并将永远作为我生命中最珍贵的手卷档案被深藏铭记。

===Original===

Thus supported humanly, and feeling the ever-near incitement and sustenance of a Presence still higher, I began to feel my way out of the barathrum of my long sojourn, and its jaws closed behind me, never since then, never hereafter till there be no more help in heaven, to open for my ingress. Out of its tremendous Elysium, its quenchless Tartarus, its speechless revelations, I came slowly into a land of subdued skies and heavier atmosphere. The jet of flame and fountain grew dimmer behind me in the mists of distance; broader, in the land from which I had long wandered, before me grew the shadows of the present life. Yet among all the lights which, unobscured by vapor, from afar led me on my way, was one which gleamed with a promise that in the days hereafter, the soul, purified from the earthy, should once more, painlessly, look on the now abandoned glories of its past apocalypse.

===Chinese===

就这样，在凡人同胞的支撑下，并感受到某种更为至高存在的永恒临在激励与扶持，我开始摸索着走出我长期羁留的深渊地狱（barathrum），它的血盆巨口在我身后轰然闭合，自此之后、直至苍天再无拯救的千古未来，皆永不再为我的踏入而洞开。从它巍峨的极乐净土（Elysium）、它难以熄灭的塔尔塔罗斯（Tartarus）深渊、它无可言状的启示神谕之中，我缓缓步入了一片苍穹低沉、大气更显凝重的广袤土地。烈火与泉水的喷涌在远方的迷雾中愈发暗淡；在我曾长期流浪背离的那片土地上，眼前当下生活的阴影逐渐拉长扩展。然而，在所有穿透迷雾自远方指引我前行的明灯之中，唯有一盏闪烁着承诺的微光：在未来的岁月里，彻底涤除了凡尘污浊的灵魂，必将再次毫无痛楚地，凝视其过往启示中如今已被舍弃的万道荣光。

## XX Leaving the Schoolmaster, the Pythagorean Sets Up for Himself / 第二十章 告别校长先生（the Schoolmaster），毕达哥拉斯门徒（the Pythagorean）自立门户
===Original===

During the progress of the events which have hitherto occupied my narrative, I had become a graduate of my college. Willing for a while to defer the prosecution of more immediately professional studies, I cast about for some employment which for a year might engage a portion of my efforts, and leave, at the same time, a reasonable amount of leisure for private reading. As is the case with so many of our newly-fledged American alumni, my choice fell upon the assumption of the pedagogic purple. There were doubtless, somewhere in the States, candidates for induction into the mysteries of the Greek and Latin tongues﻿—some youths of promise who burned for an acquaintance with the arts of address, and who would not scorn to receive, even from the hands of an own countryman, the crumbs of literature which fall from the Gallic table. If my horoscope had not failed me, I could find them out.
Accordingly, at the bar of my college, whither petitions for instruction very frequently came in from the benighted world, I lodged an application for the most eligible situation of the kind above stated which should present itself. Before long a letter reached me, offering a post of teachership, situated somewhere between the Hudson and Fort Laramie, in a village glorified with some name of Epic valor, mighty in the appanage of ten dwelling-houses and a post-office, and, like all places sanctified by the presence of the educational genius, “refined, salubrious, and highly religious.” As to the first item in this latter statement, there was every reason to be satisfied of its truth, since the writer of the letter was evidently a gentleman, and, to judge from the size of the place, he was a very large integral portion of its population. Upon the second point it was rather more difficult to be assured, since any number of deaths possible to the dimensions of the village might have occurred there without their wave of agitation reaching the shore that acknowledges the jurisdiction of bills of mortality. Finally came the question of “highly-religiousness.” On this head, nothing could have given my doubts a more decisive quietus than the fact that the community wanted a teacher, since, just then looking through the Lorraine-glass of enthusiasm for a chosen occupation, I saw a peculiar force and beauty in the words, “Science, the handmaid of Religion.”

===Chinese===

在迄今为止占据我叙述篇幅的种种事态演进之中，我已经从大学顺利毕业。鉴于我尚愿暂时搁置更具针对性的专业深造，我开始物色一份能在一年间占用我部分精力、同时又能为私人研读留出合理闲暇的工作。正如我们许多初出茅庐的美国大学毕业生一样，我的选择落在了披上教书育人的紫袍之上。在美利坚各州之中，毫无疑问正有众多渴望探求希腊语与拉丁语奥秘的莘莘学子——一些渴望通晓演说口才之术、且不耻于哪怕从同胞之手接纳自高卢餐桌上掉落的文学碎屑的有志青年。倘若我的星盘未曾欺骗我，我定能将他们寻得。

因此，在我的大学事务处——那里经常收到来自蒙昧世界的求教请愿——我针对上述类型的最合适职位递交了申请。不久，一封信函寄达我手中，向我提供了一份教席职位，地点位于哈德逊河（Hudson）与拉勒米堡（Fort Laramie）之间的某处，坐落在一座被赋予了某种史诗般英武之名的村庄里，坐拥十栋民宅与一处邮局的雄厚排场，正如所有被教育之神圣光所照耀的所在一样，号称“典雅、宜居且极具宗教虔敬”。关于最后这一声明中的第一项，完全有理由对其真实性感到满意，因为写信者显然是一位绅士，且从该村庄的规模来判断，他本人便构成了全村人口中极大的一部分。关于第二项，则相对较难确证，因为即便在村庄规模所允许的范围内发生过任何数量的死亡事件，其震荡波澜也未必能扩散触及到承认死亡公报管辖范围的文明彼岸。最后则是“极具宗教虔敬”的论断。关于这一点，最能令我的疑虑烟消云散的莫过于该社区亟需一名教师的事实——因为在彼时彼刻，透过对所选职业狂热激情的洛林镜（Lorraine-glass），我在“科学乃宗教之婢女”这一格言中看到了非凡的力量与美感。

===Original===

Yet one thing there was which stood as a slight obstacle in the way of accepting the position. I had determined, for the year to come, to be independent for a support of all aid save my own exertions. Entire self-sustenance was a very dear project with me. Could I hope for it there?
My correspondent informed me that no very great pecuniary inducements could be offered, but seductively added that, to a young man of excellent principles, who desired to establish himself as a moral centre in the community, no opening could be more promising. As he did not go on to advise me whether, in his portion of the country, “moral centres” were gratuitously fed, lodged, and clothed, besides being generously presented, as a slight token of popular esteem, with their laundry-bills, fuel, lights, and stationery, I concluded not to close with his offer, thus forever losing, for the basest of earthly considerations, the priceless opportunity of radiating circular waves of an unctuous excellence through it is impossible to tell how large an area of uninhabited timber-region. Whether any sufficiently self-sacrificing incumbent has been found to fill the rejected vacancy, from want of data is uncertain; if not, the place with the Homeric name wanders in heathen ignorance to this day.
Another application which came to me, seeming in all points satisfactory, was accepted. In the town of W﻿—﻿—, in the State of New York, a cry had gone up for a teacher, who might be absorbent as well as radiant, and one, moreover, who might indulge the hope of moderate leisure for his own self-disciplinary purposes.
There, as I began arranging matters for my departure from home, I flattered myself that a stated occupation should absorb me from regrets over the loss of my old indulgence; quiet, books, and a regular life should create in me a new stimulus and energy. The department of pedagoguery over which I was to be installed was congenial to long-consolidated tastes﻿—the ancient and the English classics. Thus I should gradually emerge out of shadows into a being with new motives, and by moderate cares blunt the pains of progress. How far I harvested my hope the sequel will show.

===Chinese===

然而，却有一件事构成了接纳该职位的些许阻碍。我已然下定决心，在未来的一年里，除了自身的辛劳之外彻底独立、不仰赖任何外在资助。完全的自给自足于我而言是一项极其珍视的夙愿。我能指望在那里实现它吗？

我的通信人告知我，对方无法提供多么优厚的薪酬诱惑，但却极其诱人地补充道：对于一位品行端正、渴望在社区中确立自身为“道德中心”的青年才俊而言，没有哪处机缘能比这里更为前途无量。由于他并未进一步向我阐明在他所在的那片国土上，“道德中心”在作为受人敬重的微薄表意而慷慨免除洗衣账单、取暖燃料、照明灯火与文具花销之外，是否能平白享有免费的食宿与衣着供给，我断然决定拒绝他的提议，从而仅仅出于凡俗最鄙陋的现实考量，便永远错失了向一片难以言喻其辽阔的无人荒莽林区辐射出一圈圈油然圣洁卓越之光的无价良机。由于缺乏史料数据，是否有某位怀具充分牺牲奉献精神的接替者填补了这一被拒的空缺尚不得而知；倘若未曾寻得，那座冠以荷马史诗般英名的所在，恐怕至今仍在一片蛮荒蒙昧中随波逐流。

寄给我的另一份求贤申请，因在各个维度上皆令人满意，被我慨然应允。在纽约州的W镇，传来了一阵对教师的渴求呼声——要求此人既能如海绵般汲取新知，亦能如明灯般启迪后学，此外，更能为自身的修身自律抱有一丝适度闲暇的期许。

在彼处，正当我开始筹备离家行装之际，我自欺欺人地坚信：一份规律的职业定能将我从对昔日旧癖丧失的懊悔中彻底抽离出来；宁静、典籍与井然有序的生活，定能在我的体内催生出崭新的动力与蓬勃的生机。我即将执掌的教席部门，恰好契合我根深蒂固的长久志趣——古希腊罗马与英格兰经典文学。就这样，我将逐步走出往昔的阴霾，蜕变为一个拥有全新动机的生灵，并通过适度的操劳来抚平前行阵痛中的创伤。我的这一期冀究竟收获了几何，随后的经历将昭示一切。

===Original===

Having reached the scene of my labors, I found myself associated with a teacher who, like myself, was a newcomer, yet not, like myself, a neophyte in the profession, for he had grown venerable in the priesthood of Minerva, having, in all probability, during his previous life, offered up numerous hecatombs of youthful victims, both male and female, upon her altar. At the same time that I congratulated myself upon possessing the aid of his experience, I discovered that I must look elsewhere for congenial sympathies, since he was one of those persons whose metal is not annealed. In youth he had indulged a happy disposition, but now saw the folly of it. Through some fault of my own early training, I was unable to discover the necessary connection between sanctity and acridity, a heart like Enoch and a face like Sphinx.
Yet upon external sympathy I did not expect to be very dependent. The institution in which I was resident offered that invaluable advantage, a large and well-selected library, where I hoped to find all those choice attachments which from without my position might deny me.
In a good library how swiftly time melts away! Not merely in the sense of its rapid passage through our absorption in other interests, but as an element in any consideration, it becomes entirely neglected. In practical business the present is our only actuality; the past has been cast down like a ladder whose rounds have helped us up to a height whence we never again expect to descend. Among books, all temporal successions are obliterated; Plato and Coleridge walk arm-in-arm; genial Chaucer and loving Elia shake hands; with them, with all, we stand enraptured upon the same plane of time, in one age, the ceaseless age of the communion of souls. Well did Heinsius say, as he locked himself into the library of Leyden, *Nunc sum in gremio saeculorum!*﻿—“Now I am in the lap of eternity!”
But gradually the increasing pressure of duties connected with my new vocation more and more deprived me of leisure for enjoying any other literature than that of textbooks.

===Chinese===

抵达我的履职之地后，我发现自己与另一位教师共事；他同我一样是初来乍到，但在这门职业中却绝非如我这般的生手，因为他在智慧女神密涅瓦（Minerva）的祭司祭坛前已然熬出了白发苍苍的资历，极大概率在他过往的岁月里，曾在女神的祭坛上献祭过无数男女青年的百牲祭品。在庆幸自己能借由他的老练阅历获益的同时，我亦发觉自己必须去别处寻觅志趣相投的共鸣，因为他是那种未经回火淬炼的顽固金石之徒。在早年他曾沉溺于快活随和的性情，如今却将其视作彻头彻尾的愚妄。或许由于我自己早期教养中的某种欠缺，我始终无法在那份神圣与刻薄之间、在如以诺（Enoch）般的心肠与如斯芬克斯（Sphinx）般的面孔之间，找出任何必然的因果关联。

然而，对于外部世界的同情相恤，我本就未曾指望过分依赖。我所任职的机构提供了无价的优渥馈赠——一座规模宏大、甄选精良的图书馆，我指望在其中寻觅到那些外在处境恐将剥夺我的一切至珍羁绊。

在藏书丰富的图书馆里，岁月消融得何其迅疾啊！不仅体现在因沉浸于其他兴致之中而浑然不觉其飞逝，而且作为任何考量中的一种维度，它被彻底抛诸脑后。在务实的事功中，唯有“当下”才是我们唯一的真实存在；而“过往”则宛若一架梯子般被弃置于地，其梯级曾助我们攀爬上了一处我们再也不指望跌落的高峰。在典籍之中，一切时间的先后序幕皆被荡涤抹平；柏拉图与柯勒律治挽手同行；风趣的乔叟与深情的伊利亚（Elia，查尔斯·兰姆）抚掌言欢；同他们、同所有的圣哲一道，我们在同一个时间层面上神魂颠倒地伫立在永恒的时代之中——那个灵魂交融神会的无休无止时代。海因修斯（Heinsius）在将自己反锁于莱顿大学图书馆时，由衷赞叹道：*Nunc sum in gremio saeculorum!*——“如今我正置身于万古的怀抱之中！”

然而渐渐地，与我的新教职相绑定的日益沉重的职责重压，愈发剥夺了我除教科书之外享受任何其他文学作品的闲暇。

===Original===

Long after the last noisy foot had pattered down the front steps of the school building did my table groan with incorrigible exercises which demanded correction, one leaf of which, laid upon the grave of any worthy﻿—Molière, for instance﻿—who spoke the language which it assassinated, would have brought up as deep a groan from the depths below as when the mandrake is uprooted.
I had promised myself regular habits; but the wanderer who was so unfortunate or so eccentric as to be shelterless at that hour, might have seen, at two or three o’clock of almost every morning, the light of my lamp shining through one of the tall windows that looked upon the street. Not that I rose early, but that I retired early﻿—in the morning. It was not the mere sense of duty and responsibility which impelled me to such labors for the school, although, indeed, these had their just, perhaps their exorbitant weight with me. An element more selfish entered into the consideration﻿—the dread of being haunted on the morrow by unappeased ghosts of business. The accumulative nature of work distressed me; the slightest thing left unfinished at the close of one day added itself to the labors of the next, and it had grown mightily during the night. There are some people so constituted that they can not slur matters if they would. No one else may notice the mint, anise, and cummin which they have forborne to tithe, but they can no more themselves overlook the deficiency than if they had neglected the weightier matters of the law.
It will be easily understood that late hours, hard work, and an almost total cessation from bodily exercise were not the best means that could have been taken to restore tone and elasticity to a mind struggling with the horrors of an abandoned stimulus. Without cares of some kind, I had doubtless been at this time a most unhappy being; yet, under such pressure as I then felt, an overtasked mind had no opportunity to recover itself, but rather grew sensitive daily to the loss of its former support. Perhaps, however, even such a state of things was better than an absence of all absorbing employment; for, although I dreaded a return to hashish as an upright man dreads the violation of his most sacred oath, I had not reached a point at which I could utterly execrate the drug.

===Chinese===

在最后一声喧闹的脚步声跑下校舍门前的台阶许久之后，我的案头依然在为堆积如山、亟待批改的顽劣习作而痛苦呻吟；只要从中抽出一张薄页，置于任何一位曾操持被其肆意践踏谋杀之语言的文豪——譬如莫里哀（Molière）——的墓碑之上，定能从九泉深处引来一声深沉至极的凄厉悲叹，恰如曼德拉草（mandrake）被连根拔起时发出的哀号。

我曾向自己许诺要保持规律的作息；然而凡不幸或因古怪脾性而在这个时刻无家可归的夜行人，几乎在每天清晨的两三点钟，皆能望见我的灯火正透过一扇俯瞰街道的高大窗户向外投射着光芒。这并非因为我起得早，而是因为我睡得早——在清晨就寝。促使我为学校如此废寝忘食劳作的，绝非仅仅是对职责与担当的责任感，尽管这些在我心中确实占据着合情合理、或许甚至是过度的分量。一种更为自私的要素潜入了这个考量之中——对次日被未竟事务的冤魂索命纠缠的深切恐惧。工作那不断累积滚雪球的特质令我寝食难安；一日终了之际遗留下的最微不足道之物，定会叠加进次日的重负之中，且在夜色中蓬勃滋长。世间有些人的天性便是如此：哪怕他们想敷衍了事也绝难做到。旁人或许根本察觉不到他们未曾献上十分之一的薄荷、茴香与莳萝，但他们自己却绝无法忽视这份缺失，恰如他们懈怠了律法中更为重大的关键纲领一般。

不难理解的是，通宵达旦、严酷劳作、以及几乎完全停止肉体锻炼，绝非让一颗正同戒断刺激物的恐怖纠缠搏斗的心灵重获健康基调与弹性的良策。倘若毫无某种操劳羁绊，在彼时彼刻我毫无疑问将成为一个极其悲惨的可怜虫；然而，在当时我所承受的这般万钧重压之下，过度透支的心智根本没有任何机会自我复原，反倒日益对丧失昔日的支撑变得更加敏感脆弱。然而，或许即便这般局面，也比全然缺乏吸引注意力的繁杂事务要好得多；因为，尽管我对重返大麻的畏惧恰如正人君子畏惧违背自身最神圣的誓言那般，但我却尚未达到能够彻底咒骂痛斥该药物的崇高境界。

===Original===

The only feat of righteous indignation which was then possible was to think ill of it, as the lover of a faithless mistress whom he must abandon, or as the patriot of his fatherland, swayed by vile rulers, when, “fallen upon evil times,” he flies it in voluntary exile. Unemployed with daily and perplexing duties, I might have heard the former siren-voice floating into my careless quiet, and, step by step, have been almost unconsciously led back into the old snares.
As it was, the fascinations of the past were hard enough to resist. If ever for a moment I granted myself leisure to sit still and think﻿—if, especially, I resigned myself with closed eyes to the train of meditations set in motion by good music, I was infallibly borne back into the hashish world, and placed face to face with its now irretrievable glories. In quick flashes the old empurpled heights for a moment broke upon me, or amid cloud battalions in their rainbow armor I floated through a tremendous heaven. Or the far windings of some wondrous river allured me into the luxuriant shadows which trembled over its brink, and I sighed for an instant with an unutterable yearning as I thought that its waves were never more to upbear my shallop of gramarye. The embodied temptation of exquisite houris swam in ethereal dance down a garden of Gul: never more were their rosy arms to embrace me. Grand temples reared their spotless pediments into a sapphire sky; a lake that answered back in its own hue the look of heaven, kissed, dimpling with a fairy laughter, the steps that ascended to their portals﻿—portals eternally barred on me. And sometimes, more solemnly alluring than all these, for an instant I caught a view of that light wherein I had of old read the sublime secrets of things by unbearable apocalypse. At such a season, well﻿—oh! unspeakably well was it that hundreds of miles stretched between me and the nearest box of hashish, for, had I possessed the means, I should have rushed to the indulgence, though it were necessary to swim a whirlpool on the way.
I made acquaintances at W﻿—﻿— who could play cunningly upon an instrument, that universal one, the piano, especially. Knowing that there was no possibility of yielding to the allurement, I, often as possible, had them play for me, while I sat almost unconscious of anything outward, abandoning myself to music-inspired visions.

===Chinese===

彼时唯一可行的义愤壮举，不过是对它心怀怨怼——宛如一个被迫抛弃背信弃义情妇的情郎，抑或一位身处恶君统治之下的爱国志士，当“生逢乱世”之际，唯有自愿流亡远遁。若非终日被繁冗复杂的教务所占据，我定会听闻昔日塞壬（siren）般的魅惑仙乐飘入我漫不经心的宁静之中，并在一阵不知不觉中，一步步被重新诱导入旧日的罗网陷阱之中。

即便在眼下的处境中，往昔的诱惑魔力亦已足够令人难以抵挡。每当我允许自己片刻闲暇静坐沉思——尤其是闭合双眸任由心神沉沦在优美音乐所唤起的一连串冥想遐思之中时，我定会被无可挽回地带回大麻的世界，并同它那如今已然无法挽回的万道荣光面面相对。在道道迅疾的闪光中，昔日的紫霞之巅瞬息间向我倾泻而下，抑或置身于身披虹彩战甲的云霄军旅之中，我在巍峨浩瀚的天宇间悠然漂游。抑或某条奇妙长河的蜿蜒远景诱惑我步入在其水畔震颤的浓郁树荫之中，而当我思索着它的波涛将永不再托举起我的魔法小舟（shallop of gramarye）时，我便在无可言状的渴慕中发出一声由衷的长叹。绝色仙女（houris）具象化的诱惑在波斯蔷薇园（garden of Gul）中跳着轻灵飘渺的舞蹈翩跹而过：她们那玫瑰色的双臂将永不再将我拥入怀中。巍峨的神庙将毫无瑕疵的三角楣山墙高耸插进蓝宝石般的苍穹；一片倒映着天容神韵的湖泊，伴随着仙子般的浅笑涟漪，轻吻着拾级而上通往殿门的层层石阶——而那些殿门，却已对我永恒闭锁。有时，比所有这些更为庄严肃穆、更为摄人心魄的，乃是我在瞬息之间窥见的那束光芒——往昔我曾在这束光中，借由令人难以承受的天启启示，通读了造化万物的崇高秘奥。在那些时刻，万幸——啊！委实是无可言状的万幸：在我与最近的一盒大麻之间，横亘着数百英里的千山万水；因为倘若我手头拥有药物，即便中途必须游过狂暴的漩涡，我也定会不顾一切地狂奔向那份沉沦。

我在W镇结识了几位精通乐器之道的熟人，尤其是钢琴这种普世之器。深知自己绝无可能向诱惑妥协，我便尽可能频繁地让他们为我演奏，而我则端坐一旁，几乎对外部的一切浑然不觉，完全任由自己沉沦在由音乐唤起的异象奇景之中。

===Original===

Yet even then, perfectly assured that I had no power to gratify the hashish appetite, I have started up from my seat to dispel by walking and the sight of familiar objects a rapture which was enchanting me irresistibly.
Constantly, notwithstanding all my occupation of mind, the cloud of dejection deepened in hue and in density. My troubles were not merely negative, simply regrets for something which was not, but a loathing, a fear, a hate of something which was. The very existence of the outer world seemed a base mockery, a cruel sham of some remembered possibility which had been glorious with a speechless beauty. I hated flowers, for I had seen the enameled meads of Paradise; I cursed the rocks because they were mute stone, the sky because it rang with no music; and the earth and sky seemed to throw back my curse.
An abhorrence of speech or action, except toward the fewest possible persons, possessed me. For the sake of not appearing singular or ascetic, and so crippling my power for whatever little good I might do, I at first mingled with society, forcing myself to laugh and to talk conventionalities. At last associations grew absolutely unbearable; the greatest effort was necessary to speak with any but one or two to whom I had fully confided my past experience. A footstep on the stairs was sufficient to make me tremble with anticipations of a conversation; every morning brought a resurrection into renewed horrors, as I thought of the advancing necessity of once more coming in contact with men and things. Any man who has felt the pangs of some bitter bereavement can understand this experience when he remembers how many a time he awoke after his affliction, and for a moment remained forgetful that it had fallen upon him. Then suddenly gathering a fearful strength, the knowledge of the reality flashed upon him, and he groaned aloud as if some fresh arrow had entered his soul. At times the awakening was so terrible an experience to me that from any other than my own hand I would have courted death as a mercy.

===Chinese===

然而即便在彼时，即便完全笃信自己根本无力满足对大麻的嗜求，我亦曾多次从座位上一跃而起，试图通过踱步徘徊与注视身旁熟悉的物体，来驱散那场正以无可阻挡之势将我俘获沉醉的狂喜。

尽管我心智中充满了繁杂的事务，沮丧忧郁的乌云却在色调与浓度上日益深沉厚重。我的痛苦绝非仅仅是消极维度的——绝非单纯为某些已逝去之物的惋惜，而是对某种实存之物的作呕、恐惧与刻骨仇恨。外部世界的客观存在本身似乎成了一种卑劣的嘲弄，对某种记忆中曾闪烁着无可名状之美的可能性的残酷伪饰。我痛恨鲜花，因为我曾目睹过极乐伊甸园那斑斓如画的原野；我咒骂岩石，皆因它们乃是沉默的死石；我咒骂苍穹，皆因它不再回荡着仙乐神韵；而大地与苍穹亦仿佛将我的诅咒狠狠反掷回我的面庞。

除了面对极少数个别人之外，一种对言语或行动的深深厌恶彻底攫住了我。为了不显得特立独行或孤僻苦修、从而削弱我原本可能施展的哪怕微末善举的能力，起初我硬着头皮混迹于社交场合，强迫自己欢笑交谈、附和着世俗的客套陈词。最终，此类交际变得彻底令人无法忍受；除了一两位我曾全盘倾吐过往经历的知心密友外，同任何其他人搭话皆需要付出惊心动魄的巨大努力。楼梯上一声轻微的脚步声，便足以令我因预感到即将爆发的交谈而战栗不已；每一个清晨的苏醒，皆是将我复活推入崭新恐怖的炼狱，因为我一想到又必须被迫同尘世的人与物产生交集的逼仄必然性。任何曾饱尝过丧亲巨痛折磨的人皆能体悟这种心境——只要他回想起多少次在罹难之后从睡梦中苏醒，有那么短短一刻完全忘却了灾难已然降临在自己头顶。随后，现实的认知骤然凝聚起可怕的力道狠狠击中他，他忍不住失声痛苦呻吟，仿佛又有一支崭新的利箭洞穿了他的灵魂。有时，清晨的苏醒于我而言是一场如此骇人的折磨，以至于只要不是出自自己的双手，我定会将死亡视作慈悲而欣然迎纳。

===Original===

The death which was but another birth and possible, the death which was utter extinction and an impossibility, seemed either of them preferable to that illusion into which the light aroused me, which men called life, but which was, after all, but death in its most horrible form, death vivified, stalking about in hollow pageantry, breathing meaningless utterances, interchanging salutations, mocking spirit by gestures without spirit, and unable to return to its legitimate corruption.
Aware as I was that this terrible state was the revenge of the rejected sorceress, and feeling it grow bitterer every day to bear, I began to struggle against two temptations, yielding to either of which seemed to offer some change of suffering, if not a permanent relief. One of these was self-destruction, the other return to hashish, and I can hardly pronounce which of the two was the most abhorrent idea. Either of them ultimately led in the same direction. My argument with myself was, that there must be some turning point, some lowest depth to the abyss which I was descending; the hope I could not see, but faith clung to it desperately, and ever kept repeating,

> “Behold! we know not any thing;

I can but trust that good shall fall

At last﻿—far off﻿—”

===Chinese===

那种作为另一种新生且完全可能的死亡，那种作为彻底湮灭且绝无可能的死亡，在我看来皆远胜于这晨曦光芒将我唤醒置身的虚妄幻景——世人将其称之为“生命”，然而归根结底，它不过是死亡最可怖的形式，是死境的还魂走肉，在空洞虚幻的排场中招摇过市，吐露着毫无意义的词句，寒暄问候，以毫无灵魂的空洞姿态亵渎着灵性，却又根本无法回归其正统合法的腐朽归宿。

深知这般骇人的境况乃是那尊被我唾弃的魔女的疯狂报复，且感受到它在日复一日的煎熬中变得愈发苦涩难当，我开始同两种诱惑展开搏斗——向其中任何一种妥协屈服，似乎皆能带来某种痛苦的转换变奏，即便不是永久的解脱的话。其中之一乃是自我毁灭，另一种则是重返大麻的怀抱，而我甚至很难断言这两者究竟哪一个念头更为可憎。它们二者最终皆通向同一条死途。我同自己的辩驳在于：在我正顺流直坠的这片深渊之中，必然存在着某个转折的节点，某种最底层的极限；那抹希望我虽无法望见，但信念却在绝望中死死抓住它不放，并在心中永不停歇地反复默念道：

> “看哪！我们一无所知；

我唯有坚信善果终将降临，

最终——在遥远的天际——”

===Original===

But, though day was terrible, night was often as much so. While indulging in hashish, none of its images had ever been reproduced in dreams, provided that I retired to sleep thoroughly restored from the last dose. Indeed, it is a singular fact, that although, previously to acquiring the habit, I never slept without some dream more or less vivid, during the whole progress of the hashish life my rest was absolutely dreamless. The visions of the drug entirely supplanted those of nature.
Now the position of things was transposed. Day was a rayless blank, night became frightful with fire. The first phenomenon which I began to notice, as I entered this condition, was the peculiar susceptibility of the brain to its last impression before my chamber was darkened. Did I look at the flame of the lamp before putting it out?﻿—for an hour afterward I lay tossing and sleepless, because one fiery spot burned unquenchably upon the surrounding blackness. Did I shut the pages of a book immediately before lying down?﻿—the last sentence I had read was as distinctly printed on the dark as it could have been upon a scroll, and there for half the night I read it till it grew maddening. Well was it for me if the words were not of gloomy import, for I could endure with measurable patience even the wearily monotonous assurance of good cheer; but one night I was forced to rise and relight my lamp to blot out the sight of such an awful sentence as this:

> “Depart, ye cursed!”

===Chinese===

然而，尽管白昼宛若炼狱，长夜却往往同样令人胆寒。在沉溺于大麻的岁月里，只要在彻底脱离前一剂药力之后就寝，它的意象便从未在梦境中复现过。事实上，这是一个奇异的事实：尽管在染上这一恶习之前，我每一次入眠皆伴随着或多或少鲜活的梦境，但在整个大麻生涯的推进过程中，我的安歇却是绝对无梦的。药物的异象彻底取代了自然的梦境。

如今，事态的格局却被彻底颠倒翻转了过来。白昼成了一片毫无光亮的死寂空白，夜晚则在烈火翻滚中变得惊心动魄。步入这一境况后我所留意到的第一个现象，乃是大脑对我寝室熄灯变暗前所接收到的最后印象具有非同寻常的敏感性。倘若我在熄灯前凝视了一眼油灯的火焰？——在随后的一个钟头里我便在辗转反侧中无法入眠，因为那一团炽热的光斑在周遭的漆黑中难以熄灭地熊熊燃烧。倘若我在躺下前阖上了一本书的书页？——我所读到的最后一句话便清晰分明地烙印在黑暗之中，宛如印刻在一卷羊皮纸上手卷之上，在半夜的时光里我反复审读它，直至令人发狂。倘若那些文字不带有阴郁凶险的意涵，对我而言尚算万幸，因为即便面对令人疲惫厌倦、千篇一律的劝勉打气之语，我亦能带着尚可承受的耐心予以隐忍；然而在某个夜晚，我却被迫起身重新点燃油灯，只为抹杀眼前所显现的这般可怖字句：

> “你们这被咒诅的人，离开我！”

===Original===

At length I used the habitual precaution, borrowed from my former usage in the hashish state, of keeping one wick of my lamp burning while I slept. At first this was very painful to my eyes; but so much better was any pain than the horror of that last permanency of the final impression, that I bore it willingly.
Gradually my rest began to be broken by tremendous dreams, that mirrored the sights and echoed the voices of the former hashish life. In them I faithfully lived over my past experience, with many additions, and but this one difference. Out of the reality of the hashish state there had been no awakening possible; from this hallucination of dreams I awoke when the terrors became too superhuman.
What has been said in an earlier part of this narrative upon the indelible characteristic of all the impressions of our life seemed to find illustration here. Doomed to reread the old, yet, though sometimes forgotten, never obliterated inscriptions, I wandered up and down the halls of sleep with my gaze fixed upon the mind’s judicial tablets. Not always were the memories in themselves painful; where of old I had felt ecstasy, in the same place I rejoiced wildly now; yet the close of that season of rejoicing was often tinged with most melancholy dye, for, from my recollection of the former order of succession, I could infallibly tell what was coming next, and many a time was it a vision of pain.
All the facts of a recalled experience took their regular relative position save one﻿—I never dreamed of taking hashish. I was always seized suddenly by the thrill; it came upon me unexpectedly, while walking with friends or sitting alone. This ignorance of any time when I took the dose did not, however, absolve me from self-convicting pangs. Invariably my first cry was, “I have broken my vow! Alas! alas!” Then followed furious exultancy. I rushed like a Maenad through colossal scenery; I leaped unhurt down measureless cataracts; I whirled between skies and oceans, both shining in fiery sapphire; I stood alone amid ruined piles as vast as the demon-built palaces of Baly. Then an undefined horror seized me. I fled from it to find my friends, but there were none to comfort me. Finally, reaching the climax of pain, I caught fire, or saw the approach of awful presences.
Then I awoke.

===Chinese===

即便在能够入眠的时刻，睡眠亦被支离破碎地割裂成一个个充满痛苦的间隙。在梦境中，我曾经历过的痛苦以极其凶险可怕的形式重现，或者伴随着全新的恶魔元素再度升华。我漫步在由阴燃木炭铺就的平原之上，头顶笼罩着燃烧着烈火般蓝宝石的低垂苍穹；我独自一人伫立在辽阔如群魔筑就的巴利神宫（palaces of Baly）般的残垣断壁废墟之中。紧接着，一种无法言说的恐怖攫住了我。我狼狈逃窜试图寻找朋友，却空无一人能予以我抚慰。最终，在痛苦达到顶峰的关头，我浑身起火燃烧，抑或目睹令人胆寒的可畏临在者步步逼近。

随后我惊醒了过来。

===Original===

But not always into the delicious comfort of a calm reality﻿—I may almost say, never; ordinarily to cry out to Heaven for the boon of an unpeopled darkness; always to find the beating of my heart either totally stopped, or so swift and loud that I could hear it with the utmost distinctness, like a rapid, muffled hammer; frequently to discover that the idea of fire had some ground in a raging fever, which parched my lips, and swelled the veins upon my forehead till they projected in relief. At such times my course was to rise and walk the floor for an hour, if need were, at the same time bathing my head until the heat was assuaged.
If memory, still blunted by the body, could thus clearly and faithfully read her old records, in what astonishing apocalypse shall they stand forth at the unerring wand of the disembodying change!
I have spoken of additions to the original scroll of visions. It remains to mention some of them.
The region around W﻿—﻿— is a limestone formation, tunneled in one place by a rather extensive and remarkable cave. I have never found there any of those lofty halls and vast stalactites which render certain other caverns famous; the calcareous depositions are very much in miniature, but some of them of a most delicate beauty. One, in particular, is a most perfect statuette (if the term may be allowed in such a connection) of Niagara Falls; the Rapids, Goat Island, with its precipitous battlement toward the lower river, the American Fall, the Horseshoe, all are there, exquisitely carved, on a scale of not quite an inch to the foot. Another is a Gothic monastery, with its shrine and Madonna just outside the grille, and a cresset hanging from the point of the portal’s arch. The chambers are often narrow and sinuous; there is nothing there to astound anyone who has visited Weyer’s Cave or the Mammoth; but as this was the only one that I had ever seen, it was there that I found my cavernous ideal.
My guide through it was a young man of the neighborhood, whose gratification in obliging a stranger was the only recompense which he would not refuse; yet dear enough was the price which I paid for my visit.
It was no less than the punishment of being cavern-haunted for weeks. Nightly I was compelled to explore the most fearful of subterraneous labyrinths alone.

===Chinese===

然而却并非每次皆能苏醒步入平静现实的甜美安抚之中——我几乎可以说，绝非如此；通常是向苍天痛苦哀号渴求一片毫无一人的黑暗赐福；总会发觉自己的心跳要么彻底停顿，要么跳动得如此急促剧烈，以至于我能无比清晰地听闻它的声响，宛若一柄急促而沉闷的重锤；常常发觉烈火的意象并非毫无根据，而是源于一场肆虐的高烧——高烧烤干了我的双唇，令我额头的血管膨胀隆起成浮雕般的凸痕。在那些时刻，我的应对之道便是起身在地板上来回踱步一个钟头（假若必要的话），同时不断用冷水冲洗头部，直至燥热彻底平息。

倘若依然被肉体所磨钝蒙蔽的记忆，尚能这般清晰而忠实地解读她昔日的旧案卷宗，那么在脱离肉体羁绊的剧变那毫不偏差的神杖指引下，它们又将在何等令人震惊的天启神示中赫然重现啊！

我曾提及过为最初异象卷轴所增添的新内容。在此尚需提及其中若干典型。

W镇周遭的区域乃是一片石灰岩地质构造，在某处被一座规模相当宏大且引人注目的溶洞所贯通凿穿。在那里我从未见过那些令某些其他名洞名扬四海的高耸殿堂与巍峨石钟乳；其钙质沉淀物在规模上极具微观微缩特征，但其中某些却具有极其精致玲珑之美。其中一处尤为特别，乃是尼亚加拉瀑布最完满的微缩雕像（倘若在此类语境下允许使用这一词汇的话）；急流、山羊岛及其朝向下游河流的险峻绝壁要塞、美国瀑布、马蹄瀑布，皆精雕细琢地呈现在那里，其比例尚不足一英尺折合一英寸。另一处则是一座哥特式修道院，在其铁栅栏外赫然耸立着神龛与圣母雕像，一座吊灯式火盆悬挂在门廊拱顶的尖端之上。溶洞的洞室往往狭窄而蜿蜒；对于凡曾游历过威耶洞穴（Weyer’s Cave）或猛犸洞（the Mammoth）的人而言，这里并没有什么足以令人瞠目结舌之处；然而由于这是我所见过的唯一一座溶洞，正是在这里，我寻觅到了自己关于洞窟的理想典范。

引领我穿行其中的向导乃是当地的一位年轻人，他在为陌生人效劳中所获得的欣慰，乃是他唯一不肯拒绝的报酬；然而，我为这次造访所付出的代价却委实极其沉重。

那代价无异于在随后长达数周的时间里遭受溶洞恶梦索命纠缠的严酷惩罚。夜复一夜，我被迫独自一人去探索那些最可怖的地下迷宫。

===Original===

Now climbing crags which gave way behind me, hanging to round projections of slippery limestone, while I heard the dislodged debris go bounding down from ledge to ledge of a yawning pit of darkness and reaching no bottom. Now crawling painfully like a worm, pushed on through winding passages no wider than a chimney, by a Fate whose will I doubted ever to bring me back. Now beholding far above my head the rifted ceiling tremble with the echo of my least footstep, in momentary agony to see it fall. Now joyfully hastening toward a glimpse of daylight, coming up to it, and falling backward just in time to save myself from plunging down some sheer wall of measureless height, upon which the labyrinth opened.
From that visit to the W﻿—﻿— cave I suffered that which only the hashish-eater and a soul in the other hell can suffer. In time, however, I slowly outgrew its memory, but only to replace it by others almost as fearful. I cite but one more in this place.
I had been sitting upon the windowsill one day, with my body partly outside, for the purpose of performing some repair upon the sash. My sleep thereafter was scared by a vision of a house supernaturally high, upon whose topmost windowsill I stood, holding on by a projecting cornice with one hand, while with the other I sought to perform some impossible purpose, which prevented it from assisting its mate. Still the cornice kept crumbling. I grasped it by a fresh projection. That also gave way; another, and that was broken by my grasp. This position was brought to a crisis in several ways. Sometimes by a powerful impulse I swayed myself inside, and the current of the dream changed. Sometimes, without my knowing how, the vision passed utterly away. Once the whole building on whose side I stood from basement to capstone took fire in an instant; I leaped to the more merciful, to avoid the more cruel death, and, awaking, found myself upon the floor in one of those feverish states of which I have spoken.
That night I slept no more. At dawn I laid myself down for an hour of disturbed slumber, to awake again to a day which was as much to be dreaded as the night.

===Chinese===

时而攀爬在身后纷纷坍塌碎裂的绝壁峭壁之上，死死攀附在湿滑石灰岩的圆形凸起上，耳畔听闻被震落的碎石顺着黑黢黢张开血盆大口的无底深渊从一道岩架弹跳滚落至另一道岩架，却永远听不见触底的声响。时而宛若蠕虫般痛苦爬行，在一条不比烟囱宽敞的蜿蜒通道中被某种我怀疑是否真愿带我回头的命运死死向前推挤。时而目睹高悬在头顶的崩裂洞顶因我最轻微的脚步声回响而剧烈颤抖，在每一刹那皆承受着眼见其坍塌砸落的极度痛苦。时而欢欣雀跃地奔向一丝破晓的日光微澜，待冲到近前，却为了避免从迷宫洞开的某道高深莫测的万丈绝壁上跌落深渊而恰好仓皇向后跌坐逃生。

从那次对W镇溶洞的造访之中，我承受了唯有食大麻者以及置身于另一层地狱的亡魂方能领教的酷刑折磨。然而随着岁月的流逝，我逐渐走出了那段记忆的阴影，但这不过是用其他几乎同样骇人听闻的记忆取而代之罢了。在此我仅再引述一例。

有一天，为了修理窗扇，我曾坐在窗台上，身躯有一部分探出窗外。此后的睡梦便被一场关于一栋高耸入云超自然楼宇的异象所惊吓，我伫立在其最高层的窗台之上，一只手死死抠住突出的檐口，另一只手却试图去完成某项不可能达成的任务，从而无法协助另一只手支撑身体。而那檐口却依然在不断崩解碎裂。我伸手抓向一处崭新的凸起。那一处亦随之崩塌；又抓向另一处，再次在我的掌心断裂。这种危局通过几种途径被推向危机的高潮。有时凭借一股强烈的冲力我将身体荡回室内，梦境的轨迹随之变幻。有时，在我不知如何达成的情况下，异象彻底消散无踪。有一次，我所附身伫立的整栋楼宇从地基到顶石在刹那间陷入了一片熊熊烈火之中；为了避免更为残酷的火刑折磨，我纵身跃向了更为慈悲的坠亡之途，在惊醒之时，发觉自己正躺在地板上，陷入了我先前述及过的那种高烧滚烫状态之中。

那一夜我再也无法入眠。拂晓时分，我躺下身来经历了一个钟头纷乱不宁的浅睡，随后再次苏醒步入了一个同黑夜一样令人畏惧胆寒的白昼。

## XXI Concerning the Doctor; Not Southey’s, but Mine / 第二十一章 论那位医生：非骚塞之医，实吾之良医
===Original===

At the time of my greatest need, I was so fortunate as to make the acquaintance of one man whose sympathy was, for months of trial, one of my strongest supports. Half discouraged in my attempts at self-rescue, I passed an hour in conversation with him, and fortitude came to me anew; for soul and its connection with the body had so long been his study that he knew how, with the utmost delicacy, to turn thought out of unwholesome channels; moreover, he had the heart as well as the brain for doing good. I need not say that he was a doctor.
I can not resist the temptation to a digression in this place for the purpose of giving my testimony, for the highest that it is worth, to one fact of past experience. It is this: if I have ever met a man before unknown to me, whose sympathies flowed instinctively toward distress, whose self-sacrifice had become an inseparable part of nature, whose comprehensive interest in all that might ennoble our kind was equaled only by his loving patience with its present infirmities, I have called him “doctor,” and, nine times in ten, have not been mistaken.
Society has now grown old enough, for the sake of self-respect at least, to despise and abandon those stale jokes upon doctors which tickled her childish ear. With her superstition of the value of a horseshoe as prophylactic against witches, let her also put aside the inanities which she talks, in her less sombre mood, of the physician in league with the sexton, and the solemnity of mock-learning which reigns over a circle of gold-headed canes. When frightened, she is ever ready to send for the doctor; she stops joking as soon as she is parturient, apoplectic from last night’s surfeit, or appalled at the consequences of having swallowed a button.
True, there are empirics in medicine. There are men who tamper with the delicate springs of life upon no other authority than that of a

> “possunt quia posse videntur.”

===Chinese===

在我最需要救助的关头，我有幸结识了一位仁兄，在长达数月的严酷考验中，他的同情相恤成了我最坚强有力的支柱之一。在自我救赎的屡次尝试中已然半心灰意冷的我，同他促膝长谈了一个钟头，坚毅的勇气便在我心中油然而生；因为灵魂及其与肉体的纽带向来是他潜心钻研的课题，他深知如何以极度精妙的拿捏将思绪引离不健康的死胡同；更有甚者，他不仅拥有敏锐聪慧的头脑，亦拥有一颗乐善好施的仁心。无须多言，他正是一位医生。

在此处我忍不住要插叙一笔，只为以其最具分量的价值，对我过往经历中的一项事实做出见证。那便是：倘若我曾遇见过某位此前素昧平生之人，其同理心本能地奔涌向苦难，其自我牺牲已然化作其天性不可分割的一部分，其对凡能升华我们人类的一切事物所抱有的广博关切，唯有在面对人类当下的种种软弱缺陷时展现出的深情耐心方能与之比拟——我定会称他为“医生”，且十之八九绝不会料错。

世人如今已然足够成熟，至少为了自尊体面起见，理当摒弃并轻蔑那些曾逗弄其孩童般双耳的针对医生的陈词滥调笑话。连同把马蹄铁视作驱除女巫灵验符咒的迷信，世人亦当抛弃在不甚阴郁的心境下闲话家常时所吐露的荒唐言语——诸如医生同掘墓人串通一气、以及在一圈金头手杖的权威下笼罩的附庸风雅的虚伪学究气。一旦惊慌失措，世人向来毫不迟疑地差人去请医生；一旦面临分娩、因昨夜暴饮暴食而中风暴毙之虞、或因误吞了一颗纽扣而惊恐万状之时，世人便会立刻收起嬉皮笑脸。

固然，医道之中确有江湖术士。确有些人仅凭一句“自信能行故而看似能行（*possunt quia posse videntur*）”的自封权威，便斗胆妄动生命精妙脆弱的发条。

===Original===

We have all seen the advertisement of one “whose sands of life have nearly run out,” and as we marvel at the length of time during which those sands have been just on the verge of their final down-flow, we are led to ask if, for the sake of that world upon which an incalculable benefit in cases of consumption may be conferred for the price of one shilling, the benevolent possessor of the recipe may not occasionally have tipped up his hourglass or diminished its aperture.
We all know the quack in medicine. We are not blind to the thousand astonishing cures of as many desperate maladies, to the placards on the highways, the columns of the press, the almanacs, the guides, the angels that come down in a hurry from heaven, calling through a trumpet to the moribund to hold on till they get there, with a bottle of syrup under each arm which shall restore peace to his afflicted family.
All these things we know; yet are there no other quacks than quacks in medicine? Are there no quacks of divinity? no quacks at law? no political quacks, that dose a diseased nation? no literary quacks, who break down the aesthetic constitution of the people? But, because Brigham Young points out the road to future blessedness through a phalanstery of wives, shall we no more go to church? Because Jeffreys was a villain, must no more causes be adjudicated? And are we to abjure all faith in the science of government inasmuch as some placeman theorizes to the mob in fustian during a campaign, or anathematize all authors in that somebody has befouled the pool of reading-mind by a volume of the *Ragpicker’s Nephew*?
If we hold faith in gold, notwithstanding base metal, let us be assured that nowhere is that gold found at a higher percentage of purity than among doctors. Where one Faun hath stolen the mantle of Esculapius, as the good sire lay sleeping, there are a hundred upon whom he has dropped it as upon worthy children.
Of all men, the doctor is to be peculiarly cherished. Let us not forget that there was one season, very early in all our lives, when without him we might not have been. Let us remember how often, uncomplainingly, he has deprived himself of sleep, of meat and drink, of all those social endearments which beautify the world to us, that we might be set at ease upon some whimsical ailment, some pulse too little or too much.

===Chinese===

我们皆曾见过某位“生命沙漏中的细沙已然快要漏尽之人”刊登的广告，而当我们惊异于那些沙粒竟然在悬于最后滑落的边缘上支撑了如此漫长的岁月时，我们不由得要问：那位宣称为了造福世人而仅凭区区一先令便能转让肺痨秘方的仁慈方剂持有者，是否偶尔会悄悄倒置他的沙漏、抑或暗中缩小沙漏的颈孔呢？

我们皆深知医界江湖骗子的伎俩。我们并未对针对同样数量绝症的上千种惊人疗法视而不见，并未对大道旁的告示招贴、报章的专栏、年历指南、以及那些从天而降匆匆赶来、用号角向垂死之人高喊挺住、双臂各夹着一瓶能让其遭难家庭重获安宁的糖浆的天使们视若无睹。

所有这些我们皆心知肚明；然而难道除了医界之外就没有其他江湖骗子了吗？神学界就没有江湖术士吗？法学界就没有骗子吗？政治界就没有给罹患重疾的国度胡乱灌药的庸医术士吗？文坛就没有摧毁大众审美体质的文字骗子吗？难道因为杨百翰（Brigham Young）借由妻妾成群的公社指明了通往未来福乐的坦途，我们便永不再踏入教堂的大门了吗？难道因为杰弗里斯（Jeffreys）法官乃是一介恶棍，世间便不再受理裁决诉讼了吗？难道只因竞选期间某位投机政客用粗俗的空话向乌合之众兜售理论，我们便当彻底摒弃对治国理政科学的全部信仰；抑或只因有人以一部《拾荒者的侄儿》（*Ragpicker’s Nephew*）的劣质书卷玷污了大众阅读的心灵池塘，我们便当将全天下的作家皆处以绝罚吗？

倘若我们在目睹劣质贱金属之余依然对真金抱持坚信，那么请深信不疑：世间没有任何领域的黄金纯度能高于医者之中。在每一位趁着良善先师酣睡之际窃取埃斯库拉庇乌斯（Esculapius）斗篷的法翁（Faun）背后，皆有一百位先师如赠予贤肖子孙般将衣钵传授与他们的真正医者。

在所有凡人之中，医生尤当获得最珍切的爱戴。切莫忘却，在我们所有人生命的最初阶段，若无他的守护，我们或许根本不复存在。让我们铭记，不知有多少次，他毫无怨言地牺牲了自己的睡眠、茶饭、以及令世间于我们而言显得绚烂温馨的一切天伦之乐，只为让我们从某种莫名的微恙小疾、或某种或快或慢的脉搏虚惊中重获释怀安心。

===Original===

When the hour of a real need calls for him, with what anxiety he watches every flush of cheek and wandering of eye, with what strategic skill he brings to an issue the battle between the forces of life and death, with what calm earnestness he throws his own energy upon our side, how with the very parental anxiety he watches hour after hour at the painful bed, with what eye of suspense he beholds the crisis come, and now, when he knows that a Greater than he has come silently into the consultation, waits until an unseen finger has touched the clogged fount of life, and given him reason to rejoice with them that do rejoice.
In a deep sympathy, in tenderness, in allowance for human frailties, there is no man who meets us on the ways of life that more resembles that mightier Physician whose cures are felt in all the arteries of the world. Like Him, the doctor is compassionate, because, measurably with Him, “he knoweth our frame, he remembereth that we are dust.” And, last of all, yet not least, be it not forgotten that there is waiting for us an hour of shadow in the Hereafter, when, all medicine failing us, save that grand one which is to cure us of the body which hath afflicted for years, the voices of farewell, mixed with weeping, that shall be heard around our pillow, will not lack one tone which hath cheered us on through so many remediable distresses, but among the last whom our closing eye shall gather in before it looks on the grand mysteries will be he who, yielding us up unwillingly to the Stronger, remains to help the beloved whom we can help no more﻿—the doctor.
It is hard to understand how any man who, like the physician, from morning till night, and often from night till morning again, is occupied with enginery and the repair of this complicated system of forces, the body, should rest contented with a mere external survey of the levers and pulleys of its machine, or the chemical phenomena of its laboratory. If he be the true man of science which his profession imperatively demands, he can not help perceiving, in a multitude of instances, that some intangible agent is working out processes for good or ill which do not array themselves under any material classification.

===Chinese===

当切实攸关性命的危急时刻召唤他时，他以何等焦灼的关切注视着面颊的每一丝潮红与眼神的每一处游离，以何等精妙的战略调度在生死两股力量的交锋中决断胜负，以何等沉着赤诚的决然将自身的全部能量投向我们这一侧的阵营，如何以父母般的深情焦灼夜以继日地守候在痛苦的病榻之侧，用何等悬心未决的目光注视着转折危机的降临；而此刻，当他深知一位比他更伟大的存在已然悄无声息地步入了会诊之席，他静静等候着，直至一只看不见的手指触碰了淤塞的生命泉眼，并赋予他充足的理由同欢欣者一同欢欣雀跃。

在深切的共情、在温柔的体恤、在对人性软弱的宽容包容之中，在我们漫漫人生长途上所邂逅的凡人里，没有谁能比医生更肖似那位伟大的至高神医——祂的疗愈伟力在整个世界的每一条生命动脉中皆被清晰感知。正如祂一样，医生充满慈悲怜悯，因为在相当程度上正如祂所洞悉的那般，“祂知晓我们的本体，祂思念我们不过是尘土”。最后，却绝非最微不足道的是，切莫忘却：在未来的彼岸正有一场阴影笼罩的时刻等待着我们，届时除了那将彻底治愈我们多年来饱受折磨的肉体的崇高大药之外，一切世俗医药皆告失效，围绕在我们枕畔听闻到的夹杂着啜泣的告别之音里，绝不会欠缺曾陪伴我们走过无数可治愈逆境的那个抚慰音调，而在我们闭合的双眸最终凝视崇高奥秘之前所收录的最后几道身影之中，定会包含着那位虽不情愿将我们交付与更强者、却依然留下来协助我们已无力照料的至亲挚爱的人——正是那位医生。

委实难以理解的是，任何像医生这样从清晨到日暮、且往往又从深夜到黎明终日专注于肉体这套精密复杂的力量系统的运转与修缮之人，怎能仅仅满足于对其机械杠杆滑轮的外在审视、抑或对其体内实验室的化学反应现象浮皮潦草地走马观花。倘若他是其职业所绝对苛求的真正科学之士，他便不可避免地会在无数实例中敏锐察觉到：某种无形的媒介力量正在为了福乐或灾咎推动着诸多演进过程，而这些过程根本无法被归入任何单纯的物质分类体系之中。

===Original===

Changes are taking place which do not seem to originate in the specific function operated upon; new elements enter the consideration of death or cure which can not be referred to food or medicine. The true physician will not be contented until he has gone back of the wheels, and investigated the nature of that strange imponderable force which is energizing them. To him the spiritual in his art is of even more importance than the bodily.
I have not, after all, been making a very wide digression, since it has just led me into the description of my friend the doctor﻿—to me, the doctor by eminence, since, spiritually, he did for my recovery that which none else could, in a lifetime, have accomplished for it corporeally.
All his life he had been communing with the great and beautiful thinkers to whom our mysterious double nature was a beloved study. Yet no man perfected in mere book-lore was he. Without seeking apologies wherewith to excuse himself from following in the train of the dogmatists of any age, he had thought for himself, and, in the possession of an inner world thus acquired, he was independent of other resources to an extent which was equaled only by my hashish kingship, and by that only in degree and not in permanency. With him the spirit of all things was as much a felt presence as their gross embodiment is to material men.
From the commencement of our acquaintance I was as much with him as the pressure of cares would allow me to be, and, when my own life had become to me a vague and meaningless abstraction, by participation with his thought and sympathy I somehow gradually drew into it an injected energy which made its juiceless pulses throb again, and awoke me out of the lethargy into which I was sinking deeper with every day. For months, but for him, the allotted course of my duties had been a mechanical round; a galley-slave, a mill-horse, could not have labored with less interest or more weariness.
As the mountain of exercises and compositions grew gradually more and more level with the plane of my table, and the evening wore on toward night, I was wont to soliloquize, “One hour more, and I will go to see the Doctor.” Once at his rooms, and the iron mantle of pedagogic restraint fell off; I was in the human character again; nay, more, I seemed to take off my body and sit in my soul.

===Chinese===

诸多正在发生的转变，似乎根本并非源于受手术干预的特定机能本身；崭新的变量介入了关乎死亡或痊愈的考量之中，而这些变量绝无法单纯归结为饮食或医药的作用。真正的医者若不追溯至齿轮转轴的背后、探求驱动其运转的那股奇异不可称量的内在生命之力的本性，便绝不会感到满足自得。于他而言，其医道之中的精神属性甚至远比肉体层面更为举足轻重。

归根结底，我并未做出多么漫无边际的离题旁涉，因为这恰好引出了对我这位医生好友的描摹——于我而言，他是卓尔不群的崇高医者，因为在精神维度上，他为我的康复所做出的壮举，乃是世间任何其他人耗费毕生精力在肉体层面上皆无法企及的。

他毕生皆在同那些将我们神秘的二元本性视作心爱研究课题的伟大卓越思想家们交融神会。然而他绝非一味死读书本、墨守成规之徒。在从未试图为自己不愿追随任何时代教条主义者的行伍寻找托辞借口的同时，他始终坚持独立思考；而在坐拥由此开辟出的内在精神世界之中，他在超然于外部资源独立的维度上，唯有我昔日的大麻王国方能与之媲美，且即便后者亦仅能在亢奋程度上、而绝非在持久稳固性上同其相提并论。于他而言，万物的内在灵性宛如粗鄙凡夫眼中有形有质的肉体皮囊一样，乃是一种切实被感知到的生动临在。

从我们结识之初起，只要事务的重压尚能允许，我便尽可能同他形影相随；当我的个人生活于我而言已然退化为一种虚妄空洞、毫无意义的抽象概念时，通过参与分享他的思想与同理同情，我不知不觉中从中汲取了一股被注入的蓬勃生机，使得那枯竭无汁的脉搏重新剧烈跳动，并将我从日复一日深陷其中的麻木沉沦中彻底唤醒。数月以来，若非因为有他的存在，我所领受的既定职责不过是一套机械麻木的例行公事；哪怕是一艘苦役船上的桨手、抑或一匹拉磨的盲马，在劳作中所怀抱的兴致亦不可能更少、所体会的倦怠亦不可能更甚。

当案头那堆积如山的习作与作文逐渐同我的桌面齐平、夜色渐深奔向子夜之际，我总习惯于自言自语道：“再坚持一个钟头，我便可以去拜访医生了。”一旦迈入他的房间，教师那冷酷如铁的刻板束缚便彻底脱落；我重新恢复了健全的人性本质；不，更有甚者，我仿佛褪去了肉体凡胎，赤条条安坐于灵魂之中。

===Original===

This very resumption of naturalness and freedom by one whose position demanded all day a peculiar self-control and reticence, will be understood by those whom fortune (or misfortune) has placed in like circumstances to be the most delicious privilege for which the tired mind can yearn. The ceasing to seem to be what he is not must always be an untold relief to anyone who has not, by long training in the necessary caution of a responsible place, utterly ceased to be what he was.
Yet the benefit conferred upon me by my acquaintance with the doctor was something more than could be comprehended in this mere exchange of the technical for the natural, the life of a profession for the life of humanity. A most kind and lively interest did he bestow upon all that pertained to my past enchanted existence, and never with more gentleness and care than he did could an own brother have supported me through the horrors wherein I was painfully journeying on my way to complete disenthrallment. By condolence, by congenial converse, by suggestion of brighter things, by indication of a certain hope in the distance if I would but press on, in a thousand ways did this friend nerve me to persistent effort, and close more tightly behind me the gates of return.
It was through his labors chiefly that I began once more to take an interest in the world, not through any renewed affection for its mere hollow forms, but for the sake of that inner essence which they embodied. Henceforth forever, after abandoning hashish, was all endurance with the external creation to be denied me unless I could penetrate deeper than its mere outside. I had known the living spirit of nature; in its husks I no longer found any nourishment, but rather the material for a certain painful loathing to expend itself upon. In my then present condition, I beheld as little beauty in the best of external things, I granted as little admiration, as any old Athenian whose eyes last fell on the divine and spirit-breathing masterpieces of Phidias, revivified to pass judgment upon some elaborately-carven gatepost.
Through the aid of the doctor I began slowly to perceive the possibility of penetrating deeper than the shard of things without the help, so dearly bought, of hashish.

===Chinese===

对于因身处特定职业而终日必须保持特殊自控与谨言慎行之人而言，重新找回自然与自由的这一过程，凡被命运（或厄运）置于类似境遇中的人们皆能深切领会：这乃是一颗疲惫不堪的心灵所能渴求的最为甜美的心灵特权。停止去扮演那个非己的角色，对于任何未曾因长期接受负责岗位的必要戒备训诫而彻底丧失本真自我的人来说，永远是一种无可言喻的莫大解脱。

然而，我同这位医生的相识所赋予我的福祉，绝非仅仅涵盖在这番由职业技术向自然本真、由行当生活向人性生活的转换之中。对于关乎我过往那段梦幻生涯的一切，他皆倾注了最为和蔼真挚的浓厚关切；在陪伴我穿越痛苦漫长、通往彻底解脱的途中所经历的重重恐怖时，纵使骨肉同胞亦绝不可能比他更为温柔细致地守护着我。通过慰勉、通过意气相投的交谈、通过对光明前景的启发点拨、通过指出倘若我持之以恒奋进便必能在前方迎来的确凿希望——这位朋友以千百种方式激励着我坚持不懈地付出努力，并在我身后将重返旧途的大门闭锁得愈发严密。

正是在他的悉心引导之下，我开始重新对世间万物产生兴致——并非出于对万物空洞外在表象的任何死灰复燃的依恋，而是为了那些外壳所具象化沉淀的内在本质。自彻底放弃大麻之后，世间一切对受造外部世界的包容接纳，于我而言皆被彻底剥夺，除非我能洞穿得比纯粹的外表更为深邃。我曾领略过大自然的鲜活灵魂；在它的空壳皮囊之中，我再也觅不得任何滋养养分，反倒唯有将其视作宣泄某种痛苦厌恶的现成素材。置身于彼时彼刻的境况之中，在外部事物最美好的表象上我所能窥见的美感，以及我所愿意施予的由衷钦佩，宛如任何一位双眼最后凝视过菲迪亚斯（Phidias）神圣生动杰作的古雅典人，在还魂之后被拉去对某根雕琢繁复的门柱品头论足时所能展现的一样贫乏吝啬。

借由医生的襄助，我开始徐徐领会到了一种可能性：无须借助代价极其沉痛的大麻，亦能洞穿外物的坚硬甲壳探寻其本质。

===Original===

Taking up, for instance, the subject of a spirit which works throughout all creation, by which the most microscopic plant-filament, no less than the grandest mountain, is inwrought and informed, we often talked together in parables, which, however, were never obscure to us, since we possessed that best dictionary of meanings, the bond of a close, congenial sympathy.
Let no one accuse us rashly of Pantheism, since it is not affirmed that we ascribed to that spirit of things divine, or in any way self-conscious attributes. Thus, as we were one day standing side by side before a window exquisitely arabesqued with trees by the noiseless graver of the frost, did the doctor discourse upon its process and its reasons:
“That the thing which men call dead matter has not wrought out this beauty is evident. The matter is here, but a more subtle force has moulded it according to hidden laws. The very necessary and primordial condition of matter is inertia, and without the touch of human hands inertia has here been overcome. Look at that palm-tree. We might shut out from our eyes its artificial frame, and all the other surroundings which connect it with man’s workmanship, and, as we gazed upon its articulate trunk, and the plumy shoots spreading from the expanded bud which forms the capital of the shaft, believe ourselves upon an oasis of Araby.
“Wherein differs this palm-tree from its brothers of the desert, the tropical garden, and the bank of Nile? In this only. The spirit of a palm has been viewlessly wandering from zone to zone in search of a body. It reaches a warm land, and there, from ammoniacal soils, from water-atoms, from numberless elements, it slowly builds about itself, in conformance to its inner laws, roots, trunk, and branches, until some way-worn Howadji throws himself down under its shadow, saying, ‘Blessed be Allah! another palm-tree.’
“A second palm-spirit, in its ethereal journeyings, comes not to the earth, but hither to this windowpane. Here it finds no soils, but only the water-drops, which all day long have been collecting from the atmosphere. Its visit is by night, and when we draw near the window in the morning, lo! the spirit has erected for itself a body of purest crystal, shaping it faultlessly, by its own unerring law, into the palm-tree which we see here.

===Chinese===

举例而言，探讨起那股贯穿于整个受造世界的灵性——从最微观的植物纤维、到最巍峨的崇山峻岭，皆无一例外地由其熔铸灌注——我们常常用寓言的方式彼此攀谈，然而这些寓言于我们而言却从未显得晦涩难懂，因为我们掌握着最权威的意蕴词典——那便是亲密无间、意气相投的心灵纽带。

切莫有人轻率地指责我们沦为了泛神论者，因为我们从未宣称将神圣的特质、或任何自我意识的属性归咎于万物的灵性。某一日，当我们并肩伫立在窗前，凝视着由严霜这位无声的雕刻家用精美绝伦的蔓藤纹饰在玻璃上勾勒出的林木图景时，医生便如是阐发了其演变过程与深层机理：

“人们所谓的死寂物质绝不可能凭空创造出这等瑰丽之美，这是显而易见的。物质本身确凿在此，但一种更为精妙幽微的伟力正依照隐藏的法则对其进行塑造熔铸。物质最根本且原始的属性乃是惰性，而在此处，未假人类双手之触碰，惰性已被彻底打破征服。瞧那一株棕榈树。我们完全可以在视线中屏蔽掉它的人造窗框、以及所有将它同人类工巧相连的周遭环境；当我们凝视着它那骨节分明的树干，以及从构成柱头的硕大花蕾中舒展蔓延的羽状新芽时，定会深信自己正置身于阿拉伯的一处绿洲之中。

“这株棕榈同它在荒漠中、热带花园里以及尼罗河畔的同胞兄弟究竟有何分别呢？区别仅在于此。一株棕榈的精魂曾无形地在各气候带之间四处游荡，寻觅着容纳自身的躯壳。它抵达了一片温暖的土地，并在那里从含氨的土壤中、从水分子中、从无数化学元素之中，依据其内在的法则，徐徐在自身周遭构建起根须、树干与枝桠，直到某位风尘仆仆的旅人（Howadji）躺倒在它的绿荫之下赞叹道：‘赞美真主！又是一株棕榈树。’

“第二株棕榈的精魂，在其超脱飘渺的漫游之中，并未降临于泥土大地，而是飘落到了眼前的这扇窗玻璃上。在此处它寻觅不到土壤，唯有整日从大气层中凝结汇聚的点点水滴。它的造访发生于子夜，而当我们在清晨走近窗前时，看哪！那精魂已然为自身铸就了一具最纯粹晶莹的水晶身躯，凭借其自身毫不偏差的法则，毫无瑕疵地将其塑造成了我们在此所见到的棕榈模样。

===Original===

“Tomorrow the spirit of the Alga may float hither for its incarnation, and on the day after the spirit of the Fern.”
Had I possessed any part in the origination of this idea, I should not venture to characterize it as I now do, singularly beautiful; yet I believe that I shall not speak without hope of sympathy in saying that such it did certainly seem to me. It chanced that in the long and severe winter which we passed together at W﻿—﻿—, my friend and I had many opportunities of beholding the verification of his prophecy, for to our windows did come frequently both Fern and Alga, with many another spirit from the universal Flora, whose filaments and petals bitter blasts only breathed into more finished perfectness, and whose fragrance was a better, a more enduring one than that of odor, since it was exhaled to the soul without mediation of corruptible organs.
As we looked upon the frost-glorified panes, our minds meanwhile tinged with this poetic theory, it was impossible to refrain from carrying up the analogy into a field which is vaster, and orbed by higher destinies than those of the unconscious creation. To a certain body of the palm alone is the breath of winter fatal. In the higher zones an incarnation reared of soils and earthy juices perishes and droops away; yet the spirit of the palm is not dead. Wafted away, it collects for itself other materials to dwell in, and crystallizes around itself a form which shall only be beautified and confirmed by that very power which destroys its other embodiment.
There is another wind in Araby, called Sarsar, the icy wind of death, which blows not upon the tree, but on the man. At its chill the bodily drops off, but the soul has never felt it. Set free by the same breath which was lethal to its shell, it voyages into another region, it crystallizes around itself “a more glorious body.” Who shall say that, to this new creation which it has informed, those very influences which worked the dismemberment of its ancient covering﻿—labor, pain, attrition, and all the thousand forces of decay, shall not the more through all the ages act to ennoble the soul, to make it a grander, better, and more harmonious being? Shall he who so clothes the grass of the field, and much rather clothes us, though of little faith, grant good uses of ill destiny to unconscious and not to conscious being?

===Chinese===

“到了明日，水藻的精魂或许会漂移至此完成其化身降临，而在后天，蕨类的精魂亦将接踵而至。”

假若这一构想中有任何一丝出自我的原创，我绝不敢如今日这般斗胆将其赞誉为超凡绝伦之美；然而我相信，当我言及它在当时确曾深深震撼了我时，绝非不期盼能获得读者的共鸣。恰逢我们在W镇共同度过的那个漫长严酷的冬日里，我的朋友与我有许多机会亲眼目睹他的预言化为现实——因为在我们的窗前，确实屡屡迎来蕨类与水藻，连同来自普世植物界的万千精魂；严寒的凛风仅将其纤细的丝络与花瓣吹拂雕琢得愈发臻于化境，而其馨香乃是一种比凡俗嗅觉更为崇高、更为恒久的馨香，因为它是无须经过凡胎肉体易朽器官的媒介折射、直接向着灵魂所喷薄吐露的。

当我们凝视着这些被冰霜镀上荣光的窗玻璃，心神同时被这套诗意哲思所浸润之时，我们无法抑制住将这一类比推演提升至更辽阔领域之中的冲动——一片远比无意识造化更为宏大、由更高阶命运所环绕的宏伟疆域。唯有对于棕榈的某具特定肉身而言，冬日的吐息才是致命的打击。在较高纬度的寒带，由土壤与泥水汁液滋养出的化身肉体固然会凋零消亡；然而棕榈的精魂却绝未死去。随风飘荡远去，它为自身汲取了其他赖以栖居的质料，并在自身周遭凝结晶化出一种全新的形态——而这一形态，唯独会被摧毁其另一具化身躯壳的同一种严寒伟力所美化加固。

在阿拉伯的传说中，存在着另一种名为萨尔萨尔（Sarsar）的风，那是一阵象征死亡的刺骨寒风，它吹拂的并非树木，而是凡人。迎着那股刺骨的寒意，肉体的外壳纷纷剥落，但灵魂却从未感受到丝毫侵袭。借由对其躯壳而言乃是致命打击的同一股吐息而重获自由，灵魂远航步入了另一方天地，在自身周遭凝结晶化出一具“更为荣耀的身躯”。有谁能断言，对于这具由其所赋予生机的全新造化而言，那些曾推动其昔日躯壳土崩瓦解的磨砺力量——劳苦、痛苦、砥砺消磨、以及千百种衰朽溃败的伟力——难道在千秋万代之中，不会反倒更进一步发挥升华灵魂的功效，使其蜕变成为一个更为宏伟、更为崇高、更为和谐的完美存在吗？那位既然如此装扮田野里的花草、且即便我们信心微弱亦更有理由装扮我们的至高主宰，难道只会将恶运的良善功用赋予无意识的造化，而唯独吝于赐予具有灵性自觉的存在吗？

===Original===

As a legitimate and by no means unexpected consequence of our living somewhat in seclusion, and holding both opinions and converse which were not absolutely universal, there were not wanting those who dubbed us visionary, the severest epithet of reproach which can be hurled by A, whose horizon of interests is bounded by beef and clothes, at B, who inquires within a wider scope. I do not remember that we ever writhed very convulsively under this fearful thunderbolt, but bore it as became not altogether annihilated, good-humored martyrs.
As we talked of this subject upon a certain evening, thus spake the doctor in parable:
“Once upon a time there abode in a bar of iron two particles of electricity. Now one of these particles, being of an investigating temperament, to the great discredit of his family, and the shame and confusion of face of all who held high seats in the electric synagogue, set out upon a wild voyage of discovery. For a long time he was absent, and, as no tidings came from him, it was supposed he had perished ignominiously at the negative pole. In the mean while, the other particle of electricity, who stayed at home and minded his own business, by gradual accretions had secured to himself size and dynamic consideration in the community. After the lapse of several seconds (which must be known is a long period to individualities which travel as rapidly as the electric) the erratic particle returned, and visiting his friend, the particle who had attained a position of high respectability, happened to let fall in conversation this remark: ‘I have discovered in my journeyings that we are not the only beings extant, but that, in fact, we live in and are surrounded by a body called iron, which, from our difference of state, it possessing a far greater density than we, we do not perceive.’
“Thereat the other particle waxed wroth, and muttered something like ‘humbug!’ But the traveler, pressing the claim of his new fact, did so excite his respectable friend that he broke forth thus: ‘Do you pretend to belie the evidence of my senses? All my life I have been going up and down about my business, and have never yet seen, heard, smelt, tasted, or felt such a thing as iron in the whole time. Why don’t I run my head against it?’

===Chinese===

作为我们过着某种离群索居的生活、且抱持着绝非普世认同的观点与谈吐的合情合理、且绝非出人意料的后果，周围自然不乏有人将我们蔑称为“耽于空想的狂人”（visionary）——这是由兴趣眼界仅局限于牛肉与衣裳的A君，向着在更广袤维度内求索真理的B君所能掷出的最严厉的谴责标签。我不记得我们曾在那记可怕的晴天霹雳下痛苦抽搐过，反倒以一种未曾被彻底消灭、幽默豁达的殉道者姿态泰然处之。

在某个傍晚当我们探讨起这一话题时，医生便讲了如下一则寓言：

“从前，在一根铁棒内部寄居着两颗电微粒。其中一颗微粒天生具有探究求索的性情，它全然不顾家族的颜面蒙羞，亦不顾在电气会堂中高坐显位的所有长辈的羞愧难堪，毅然踏上了一场狂野的探险发现之旅。他离开了漫长岁月，且因毫无音信传来，大家都以为他早已可耻地命丧于负极之中。与此同时，另一颗留守家园、安分守己操持自身生计的电微粒，凭借循序渐进的资本积累，在社群中赢得了庞大的体量与显赫的动态声望。在经历了数秒钟的时间流逝之后（读者必须知晓，对于像电这般以光速飞驰的个体而言，数秒已是一段漫长的岁月），那位游历四方的微粒归来了；在拜访他那位已然跻身德高望重显赫地位的朋友时，碰巧在交谈中随口吐露了这番感慨：‘我在漫长的旅途中发现，我们绝非世间仅存的存在，事实上，我们正生活在一具名为“铁”的物体内部并被其团团包围，只是由于我们状态维度的差异——它所拥有的密度远高于我们——导致我们平日里根本察觉不到它的存在。’

“听闻此言，另一颗微粒勃然大怒，嘴里嘟囔着诸如‘胡说八道！’之类的字眼。然而那位旅行者坚持重申他发现的全新事实，这激怒了他那位体面的朋友，以至于后者忍不住失声咆哮道：‘你竟敢妄图否定我感官的明证吗？我这一辈子都在为自己的营生奔波劳碌，在整段岁月里，我何曾看见、听见、闻到、尝过、或摸到过哪怕一丝所谓“铁”的东西？我怎么就没一头撞死在它身上呢？’”

===Original===

Since that day, it is credibly stated that whenever the practical particle stands on ’Change talking with other practical particles, and the inquiring particle comes along, the former shakes his head, and says to his friends, ‘Unreliable﻿—talks nonsense about a crotchet which he calls iron﻿—visionary, very visionary.’ ”

===Chinese===

自那一刻起，据说有确凿证据表明：每当那颗务实的微粒伫立在交易所（’Change）同其他务实的微粒高谈阔论、而那位善于探究求索的微粒漫步经过时，前者总会连连摇头，对身旁的朋友们说道：‘此人不可靠——整天满嘴胡言，唠叨着一个被他称作“铁”的古怪偏执念头——空想家，纯粹是一个耽于空想的狂人。’ ”

## XXII Grand Divertissement / 第二十二章 华丽消遣
===Original===

As the months went on, the fervor of my longing toward the former hashish life in some measure passed away, and in general the fascination to return did not present itself so much in the form of pining for an affirmative as loathing of a negative state. It was not the ecstasy of the drug which so much attracted me, as its power of disenthrallment from an apathy which no human aid could utterly take away. Yet even now there were seasons of absolute struggle in which I fought as against a giant, or more truly to the nature of things should I say, in which I resisted as against a demon houri, for my tempter was more passing lovely than anything on earth.
As in the earlier period of my warfare, I now and then caught glimpses of ravishing delight, which, through some rift in the thick cloud elsewhere completely enveloping my daily life, broke in upon me for a moment, yet lasted long enough to prove that I could not yet write myself secure, that my integrity was not yet beyond corruption.
Some of my readers will doubtless be amused, others pained, and a few disgusted at the childlike expedients to which I found it necessary to resort for the purpose of appeasing this renewed appetite for visions without a return to hashish. There were three different sets of circumstances which almost infallibly brought on the longing. It was never suggested by dark and stormy weather, since this was too much in consonance with my habitual mood to demand more than a passing notice. The man who has lost an intimate friend does not pay much attention to murk and mist; it is sunshine which seems to mock his melancholy. So in my own case did it happen. The season of most intense longing was a day of clear sky and brilliant light. That beauty which filled the heart of every other living thing with gladness, only spoke of other suns more wondrous rolling through other heavens of a more matchless dye. I looked into the sky, and missed its former unutterable rose and sapphire; no longer did the whole dome of the firmament sound with grand unwritten music.
It was a pain to look into that desert wilderness of blue which of old my sorcery had peopled for me with innumerable celestial riders, with cities of pearl and symphony-haunted streams of silver. I shut my eyes, and in a moment saw all that I had lost.

===Chinese===

随着数月的流逝，我对往昔大麻生活的狂热向往在相当程度上逐渐消退，且大体而言，重返旧习的诱惑不再表现为对某种积极体验的渴望，而是化作了对某种消极状态的厌弃。吸引我的与其说是该药物所带来的狂喜，倒不如说是它能将我从任何人力皆无法彻底消除的麻木冷漠中解脱出来的超拔之力。然而即便到了此时，依然时有绝对苦战的关头——在那些时刻，我宛若同泰坦巨人搏斗，或者更契合事物本质地说，我宛若在抵抗一位女恶魔胡里仙女（houri）的魅惑，因为我的诱惑者比尘世间任何凡物都更为美艳绝伦。

正如在我这场战争的早期阶段一样，我时不时仍会瞥见勾魂摄魄的极乐欢愉——透过在其他方面完全笼罩了我日常生活的那团厚重乌云的一道裂隙，它在瞬息间向我奔涌袭来，尽管稍纵即逝，却足以证实我尚不能自诩安全、我的节操操守尚未完全超然于诱惑腐蚀之外。

读者诸君中，凡读到我为满足这种对异象死灰复燃的渴望、同时又避免重返大麻而不得不采取的孩童般的权宜之计，毫无疑问有人会感到忍俊不禁，有人会倍感心酸，亦会有少数人嗤之以鼻。大体有三类不同的环境境遇几乎必然会唤醒这种渴望。它从未由阴沉暴烈的风雨天气所诱发，因为那种天气同我习以为常的心境过于吻合，以至于除了泛泛留意之外引不起更多波澜。痛失挚友之人绝不会对阴霾迷雾投以过多关切；反倒是明媚的阳光似乎在嘲弄他的忧伤。在我自己的经历中亦是如此。诱惑最为强烈的时刻，乃是万里无云、艳阳高照之日。那份让周遭每一个生灵心中充盈欢欣的美景，却只在向我诉说着其他更为奇妙的太阳正穿行于其他拥有更无可匹敌色泽的浩瀚天宇。我仰望苍穹，怀念起它往昔那无可言状的玫瑰色与蓝宝石华彩；整个天宇穹顶已不再回荡着宏大未谱写进乐谱的仙乐神曲。

凝视着那片蔚蓝的荒漠旷野于我而言乃是一种痛苦，昔日我的巫术曾为我在其中布满了无数天界的骑兵、珍珠铸就的城池与回荡着交响乐的银色溪流。我阖上双眼，刹那间便看见了自己所痛失的一切。

===Original===

A night of brilliant moonlight brought me other repentings after my enchanted life, whose tone was not so high as those of the sunshine, but deeper and more enduring. Wrapped in a melancholy which could not be imparted, I wandered by the hour through the beaming streets, and looked sadly around me to see the meanest object by the wayside

> “Change

Into something rich and strange.”

===Chinese===

明月高悬的皎洁月夜，则为我带来了另一类对往昔梦幻生活的悔恨追忆，其基调虽不及烈日之下那般激昂高亢，却更为深沉持久。裹挟在无法与人分享的忧郁之中，我连续几个钟头漫步在波光粼粼的街道上，凄然环顾周遭，目睹路旁最卑微鄙陋的物体

> “化作

奇异而瑰丽的珍宝。”

===Original===

The stones beneath my feet gleamed like unhewn crystals. The frosty fretwork on the panels of doors which I passed, at the touch of the divine Moon-Alchemist became exquisite filigrees of silver. The elm-trees and the locust, shedding sparkles of radiance from their ice-encased twigs, might well have been those trees of gleamy ore which Allah buried when man was cast out of Paradise.
Yet mournfully I thought of the old days, when I would have walked down these shining ways as through an ever-lengthening vista of glories, when the moonlight would have fallen on me mysteriously empurpled, when over all the wondrous domain I had felt myself unquestioned sovereign, and out of the beauteous recesses of earth and sky sprite voices had musically hailed me to my kingdom.
As I thought upon these things, now forever irretrievably abandoned to the past, I have wept﻿—yes, though it be unmanly, I have wept to find myself a discrowned king, a sorcerer ravished of his wand, a god shorn of his glories. I am not ashamed to remember that I did this; for if there be any ecstasy possible which we do not now feel imparted to us, if any excellency in things which does not now make itself tangible, it is no more ignominious to lament over it perished than to sigh after it tarrying.
There was another, a bodily condition, which I always found it necessary to avoid if I would not be smitten with repinings after the hashish life. It was the nervous sensitiveness induced by deprivation of tobacco.
In smoking, if in nothing else, could I boast regularity of habit. To be sure, for this regularity neither an unusually developed organ of order nor the possibility of anything like a systematic arrangement in my multiplicity of labors was to be preeminently thanked. To defer for an hour the nicotine indulgence was to bring on a longing for the cannabine which was actual pain. When circumstances have occurred which made it impossible to smoke before entering my daily round of duties, until they closed I have hardly dared to shut my eyes, lest I should be borne incontinently out of the actual life into which necessity called me to a land of colossal visions. If for a moment I yielded to the impulse, I was straightway in the midst of sky and landscape whose splendors were only less vivid than the perfect hallucinations of the fantasia.

===Chinese===

我脚下的石块宛如未经雕琢的水晶般熠熠生辉。我走过的一扇扇门扉嵌板上那如霜雪般的镂空花纹，在神圣“月光炼金术士”的轻抚之下，化作了精妙绝伦的纯银掐丝细工。榆树与刺槐在被冰霜包裹的枝杈上散发着点点晶莹璀璨的光华，宛若当年人类被逐出伊甸园时真主安拉（Allah）埋入地底的那些流光溢彩的矿脉神树。

然而我却悲凄地回想起昔日的岁月——在那些日子里，我曾漫步走下这些闪烁着光芒的坦途，宛如穿行在一条不断延伸延展的荣耀远景之中；每当彼时，月色皆神秘莫测地以紫霞倾泻在我身上；在那片奇妙的领地之上，我曾感到自己是无可置疑的至尊主宰；而从大地与苍穹那瑰丽幽深的隐秘之处，精灵的嗓音曾以天籁仙乐恭迎我君临王国。

每当思及这些如今已永远无可挽回地遗落入往昔的事物，我都忍不住怆然泪下——是的，尽管这显得不够男儿气概，但发现自己沦为了一位被剥夺王冠的废王、一位被夺走魔杖的巫师、一位被剪去万道荣光的神明，我依然潸然泪下。忆及当年的这番举止我并不感到羞愧；因为倘若世间确有某种我们如今未曾感受赋予我们的可能狂喜，倘若造化之中确有某种如今未曾显露真容的卓绝完美，那么为其消逝而悲叹，绝不比为其姗姗来迟而长吁短叹更为可耻。

此外，还有另一种肉体层面的状态，倘若我不想被对大麻生活的苦苦怨念所击倒，我便发觉必须时刻竭力避免。那便是因剥夺烟草所诱发的神经敏感脆弱。

在吸烟这件事上，倘若在其他事情上尚且不能的话，我至少能自诩保持着规律的习惯。诚然，对于这份规律性，既不归功于某种异常发达的秩序感官，亦不归功于在我千头万绪的繁重劳作中可能存在的任何系统化安排。将享受尼古丁的时刻推迟哪怕一个钟头，便会引来对大麻素（cannabine）的强烈渴望——那是一种切实的痛苦。每当遇到某些突发境况使我在步入每日例行公事之前无法抽上一斗烟时，在工作结束之前我几乎不敢阖上双眼，生怕自己会身不由己地从现实生活——正是生存必然性召唤我投身其中的生活——被裹挟而去，坠入一片充斥着宏大异象的魔法国度。倘若我哪怕有一刻向这股冲动妥协屈服，我便会立即置身于苍穹与山川之中，其璀璨壮美仅次于奇想全盛时期的彻底幻觉。

===Original===

But I have not yet spoken of those expedients to which I resorted for relief and to avoid the necessity of resuming the use of hashish. Certainly, in them ingenuity, so far as I possessed any, was tortured to its utmost endurance.
Sometimes I spent the few moments of leisure which during the day could be snatched from business in﻿—mention it not confidently in Gath, breathe it not to the friend of thy bosom in Askelon﻿—blowing soap-bubbles. Not that there is aught deserving of contempt in the enjoyment of that which has been made a philosophic toy by one of the greatest of Anglo-Saxon sages﻿—not that the pleasure of rare beauties from humble elements is of necessity an aesthetic heresy, but because the hashish-eater is well aware of the existence of critics, to whom all that is childlike is also childish, who quarrel with men for being perversely happy on moderate means, and with their Creator because he has not made all the little hills as high as Cotopaxi.
Yes, throwing down the wand of professional majesty, degrading myself to the level of the most callow neophyte of an infant class, did I take up the pipe, and, going into the presence of the nearest sunbeam (a course which, by the way, might well be followed by those who for their light go farther and fare worse), did I create sphere after sphere, not, as the grotesquely but unintentionally blasphemous old poet hath it, snapping them off my fingers into space, but with careful hand taking rest over the back of a chair to counteract the tremulousness of over-anxiety not to tremble, did I inflate them to the maximum, and then sit wrapped up in gazing at their luxuriant sheen until they broke.
There I found some faint actualization of my remembered hashish-sky, and where the actual failed there did the ideal, thus stimulated, come in to complete the vision. Had time allowed me, I could have consumed hours in watching the sliding, the rich intermingling, the changes by origination, and the changes by reaction of those matchless hues, or hues at least so matchless in the real world that to find their parallel we must leave the glories of a waking life, and go floating through the firmament of some iridescent dream. Verily, he who would be meet for the participation in any joys must robe himself in humility and become as a little child.

===Chinese===

然而我尚未述及那些为了寻求慰藉、并避免被迫重拾大麻而采取的权宜良策。毋庸置疑，在这些举措之中，我的聪明才智（倘若我尚有一丝的话）已被榨取折磨到了承受力的极限。

有时，我将白天从公务中好不容易偷得的片刻闲暇，用于——切莫在迦特（Gath）公然宣扬，切莫在亚实基伦（Askelon）向你的知心挚友吐露——吹肥皂泡。这绝非因为在一件被盎格鲁-撒克逊最伟大的圣哲之一视作哲思玩具的事物中寻欢作乐有何值得鄙夷之处——亦非因为从卑微的元素中发掘罕见之美必然是一种审美异端，而是因为食大麻者深知世间充斥着这般批评家：在他们眼中，一切具有赤子之心的事物皆是幼稚无知的表现；他们会因人们凭借微薄的物质条件便固执地感到幸福而大动干戈，亦会因造物主未曾将世间所有的矮丘皆造成如科托帕希火山（Cotopaxi）那般雄伟而向上苍怨声载道。

是的，扔下职业威严的神杖，将自己贬低至幼儿园学童中最为不谙世事的黄口孺子的水平，我拿起了吹管；走到距离最近的一束阳光前（顺便提一句，那些为了追寻光明而舍近求远、结果境遇更为糟糕的人，大可效仿这一做法），我吹出了一枚又一枚球体；我并非如那位荒诞怪异却并无亵渎之意的古诗人所写的那样、用手指将它们轻弹入虚空之中，而是用谨慎的手稳稳扶在椅背上以抵御因过度唯恐颤抖而产生的战栗，我将它们吹胀到最大的极限，随后整个人痴痴沉醉于凝视它们华美绚烂的光泽，直至它们破裂消散。

在彼处，我寻觅到了对自己记忆中大麻苍穹的些许微弱具象，而凡在现实有所欠缺之处，由此受到激发的理想之境便随即介入，补全了整场异象。倘若时光允许，我能在那里耗费数个钟头，痴痴注视着那些无双色彩的流转滑动、绚烂交融、因源生而起的变化、以及因互激而生的变幻——抑或至少是在现实世界中如此举世无双的色彩，以至于若要寻觅其对照，我们必须舍弃清醒生活的种种荣耀，飘然而去穿行于某场虹彩斑斓的梦境天穹。诚然，凡欲配得上分享任何欢愉之人，皆必须以谦卑为袍服，让自己蜕变成为一名小小的孩童。

===Original===

There was one other way in which I measurably reproduced the past for my innocent satisfaction. Had I permitted, at certain seasons, any foreign eye to invade the sanctity of my room, it would have fallen, possibly with some surprise, upon a singular arrangement of the books upon my table into a form somewhat resembling those houses which children build at their play. Yet the stranger would have very little suspected a clue to the mystery in the fact that I had thus been embodying to myself the ideal of the ancient cavern or the resplendent temple in which many a day ago I had exulted through a whole evening, while the rocks echoed with strange music, or oracular voices spoke to me out of the inner shrine. Had he asked me the secret, he had probably not been much the wiser for my answer.
There is still another method, and by far the most efficient of all, by which I gratified the visionary propensity without returning to the old indulgence. I had been advised by the counselor to whose article I originally owed my emancipation, whenever the fascination of the drug came upon me with peculiar power, to evade it by reenacting some former vision upon paper. A truly wise and well-considered counsel did I find this, and one which, whenever the possibility existed from any gap in my daily occupation, I followed scrupulously. As would have resulted from once more superinducing the hashish delirium, my visions, marshaled out of memory, marched past beneath varying banners; some of them banded under hell-black flags, and others carrying the colors of a rainbow of the seventh heaven.
From this reproduction of the past in the order in which it had occurred, I gained a double benefit, the pleasure of appeasing the fascination without increasing it, and the salutary review of abominable horrors without any more than the echo of a pang. In this way some portion of the present narrative was sketched at first, but of necessity a very small one, since the pressure of business made my abode, even in the most innoxious dreamland, that only of a wayfaring man who turneth aside but for a night.

===Chinese===

还有另一种方法，能让我在适度范围内重现往昔以获得无伤大雅的慰藉。倘若在某些特定的时刻，我允许任何外人的目光侵入我房间的私密圣殿，那目光或许会带着几分诧异，落在我案头书籍的一种奇异摆放方式上——其形态颇似孩童在嬉戏时搭建的小房屋。然而那位陌生人绝不会猜透其中的玄机线索：我其实正以此向自己具象化那座古老的洞穴、抑或那座金碧辉煌的神庙的理想之境——在多日以前，我曾整整一个傍晚在其中欢欣雀跃，而岩石间回荡着奇异的仙乐，神谕般的话音从内殿深处向我娓娓道来。倘若他向我探问其中的玄机，我的答复恐怕也无法让他变得更明智几分。

还有另一种方法——且是迄今为止所有方法中最为行之有效的一种——能让我在不必重返旧习沉沦的前提下，满足对异象的嗜好偏向。我最初蒙其文章启发而获得解放的那位导师曾告诫我：每当药物的诱惑带着特殊的魔力向我袭来之时，便通过在纸面上重现昔日的某场异象来规避摆脱它。我发觉这是一剂真正睿智且深思熟虑的良方，凡在日常事务的间隙中存在哪怕一丝可能，我都一丝不苟地遵从践行。宛若再度诱发大麻谵妄所必然导致的结果一般，我的异象从记忆的队列中被检阅集结，在各式各样的旗帜下鱼贯走过；其中某些在漆黑如地狱的旌旗下列队进发，另一些则高擎着来自第七重天的七彩虹霓之色。

借由依照昔日发生的顺序对过往进行这番重现，我获得了双重的福祉：既获得了平息诱惑而不使其滋长蔓延的欣悦，亦收获了对可憎恐怖之事的有益回顾检视，且除了剧痛的一丝微弱余响之外别无创伤。通过这种方式，本书叙述的某些片段最初便得以草就成形，但必然只是极其微小的一部分，因为公务的紧迫使得我即便身处最无害的梦境之乡，亦不过是一名仅借宿一晚便匆匆折返的过路行者。

## XXIII The Hell of Waters and the Hell of Treachery / 第二十三章 恶水地狱与背叛地狱
===Original===

It is not to be supposed, however, that, with all these expedients, I was now leading a life of quite tolerable calm; on the whole, rather enviable for its ideal diversions, and free from most of those sufferings which, at its abandonment, if not before, Nature sets as her unmistakable seal of disapprobation upon the use of any unnatural stimulus. If, from a human distaste of dwelling too long upon the horrible, I have been led to speak so lightly of the facts of this part of my experience that any man may think the returning way of ascent an easy one, and dare the downward road of ingress, I would repair the fault with whatever of painfully-elaborated prophecy of wretchedness may be in my power, for through all this time I was indeed a greater sufferer than any bodily pain could possibly make me.
For many a month my nights, or whatever portion of them was given to sleep, were tormented with terrific visitations. After a time Niagara began again especially to haunt me. In every variety of dangerous posture, helpless, friendless, frequently deserted utterly of every living being, I hung suspended over the bellowing chasm, or slid down crumbling cliffs toward the treacherous pavement of ever-shifting emerald. But one consolation ever broke in upon my distress; it was that stony face, which mutely shared with me, beneath its everlasting veil, the terror of the waters. Could I but crouch beside it in my dream, one element was wanting to my utter isolation.
Yet it was not invariably for myself alone that I feared. Sometimes a tremendous ship came floating up the river without a sign of life upon its decks of man or beast. Against the current it made headway without wheels or sails, but on coming to a certain place always stood still. I soon learned to foretell what was next coming, so that I groaned in the consciousness of an infallible prophecy of evil. A shudder shook the river, as if some dire convulsion was breaking up from its measureless abysses, and then slowly did the giant vessel begin to sink, bow foremost. Slowly she settled till her fore-chains were out of sight; then came a tumultuous surging outcry of despair; the decks, the shrouds, the stays were populous with human beings, unseen until that moment of ruin, and still clinging with iron clutch to those vain supports for the life which could not last.

===Chinese===

然而绝不可妄下定论，以为凭借所有这些权宜之计，我此刻便过上了一种相当尚可忍受的宁静生活——大体上因其理想化的精神消遣而颇令人艳羡，且免除了大自然在人们戒绝（若非在戒绝之前的话）任何非自然刺激物时作为其确凿无疑之否决印记所施加的大多数痛苦折磨。倘若出于凡人对过分流连于恐怖之事的天生反感，导致我将这部分经历的事实叙述得过于轻描淡写，以至于让任何人误以为重返光明的攀升之途轻而易举、并斗胆去尝试步入深渊的下坡路，我愿竭尽全力用极尽沉痛阐述的悲惨预言来弥补这一过失——因为在整段时光之中，我所承受的苦难确实远非任何肉体剧痛所能企及。

在长达数月的岁月里，我的夜晚——抑或其中凡被交付给睡眠的时间片段——皆饱受骇人听闻的幽魂梦魇的折磨侵扰。一段时间之后，尼亚加拉尤其再次开始对我穷追不舍。在各种危险至极的体态姿势下，孤立无援、毫无朋友、往往被世间一切活物彻底遗弃，我悬垂在咆哮轰鸣的深渊之上，或顺着崩塌碎裂的绝壁向下滑落，跌向那不断变幻、险象环生的翡翠色水面石板。然而在我的痛苦之中，唯有一丝慰藉曾破土而入；那便是那张石魄雕凿的面容，它在永恒的面纱之下，默默同我分担着水流的恐惧。在梦境中我只要能蜷缩在它的身侧，我的彻底孤寂便至少缺少了一项绝望的要素。

然而我所畏惧的，却并非每一次皆仅仅关乎我自己。有时一艘巍峨的巨舰逆流而上漂浮在河流之中，其甲板之上看不到任何人类或牲畜的生命迹象。它未假车轮亦未张风帆便顶着激流逆水前行，但每每行至某一特定地点便定会戛然而止。我很快便学会了预判接下来即将发生的祸事，以至于在对灾难确定无疑的预言自觉中忍不住痛苦呻吟。一阵战栗震颤了整条江河，宛若某种可怕的剧烈抽搐正从其深不见底的无底深渊中崩裂而出；紧接着，那艘庞然巨舰缓缓开始以船艏为先向下沉没。它徐徐下沉，直至其前斜桅支索没入水下无影无踪；随后爆发出一阵绝望混乱、汹涌奔腾的呼号惨叫；甲板上、缆绳间、支索处顿时挤满了密密麻麻的人群——直到这毁灭的关头他们此前皆不可见，此刻依然带着铁铸般的死力紧紧抓握住那些虚妄的依凭，徒劳地维系着无法长存的性命。

===Original===

I saw them, one by one, lapped in as the green water mounted, and with the last bubble of their dying breath the main truck disappeared, and a moment more saw the river sliding onward as before.
I have no idea how many times sleep rang changes of horror upon that dreadful dream, but often enough, indeed, to make me shudder with a speechless pang whenever water flowed or a ship drifted into the vast area of my nightly vision.
Gradually it grew the habitual tendency of my dreaming state to bring all its scenes, whether of pleasure or of pain, to a crisis through some catastrophe by water. Earlier in the state which ensued upon my abandonment of hashish I had been affrighted particularly by seeing men tumble down the shafts of mines, or, as I have before detailed, either dreading or suffering some fall into abysses on my own part; yet now, upon whatever journey I set out, to cross the Atlantic or to travel inland, sooner or later I inevitably came to an end by drowning, or in the imminent peril of it.
