It seemed singular to me, in the waking state, that I never made use of past experience, during terrific dreams, to assure myself that a certain danger was only imaginary. Before abandoning hashish, in natural dreams I had frequently employed the power of logical deduction﻿—which, in the case of many persons, remains tolerably active during sleep﻿—saying to myself, “You were frightened by this same danger before, and it turned out to be only ideal after all;” upon which I immediately awoke, or beheld the danger pass away.
Aware of this fact, I often determined, in the daytime, to rouse myself from the distresses of the night by the same expedient, but when they came it was never once thought of. That law of hashish operation by which all existence is merged in the present, and there is no memory of having ever lived in a previous state, was most consistently obeyed by the sleeping horrors of abandonment. There was no way so much as conjectured by which the spell of reality might be broken, and the determination of the day being thoroughly ignored, the only remedy was to endure unto the end.
Yet there was one most agonizing vision, whose close proved an exception to the ordinary watery catastrophe, and which stamped itself upon my mind with a vividness lingering, even while I was awake, for many days. It also, like so many of the rest, was connected with Niagara.

===Chinese===

我目睹着他们随着碧绿水流的上涨被逐一吞没，伴随着他们临死喘息的最后一个气泡泛起，主桅顶端的大顶帽亦消失在水下，片刻之后，但见江河正如先前一样从容滑过奔流向前。

我根本无法知晓睡眠在那场可怕的梦境上究竟翻新上演了多少次恐怖的变奏，但委实频繁得足以让我在每逢江水奔流或巨舰漂入我夜间视野的广袤天地时，皆因一阵无可言状的剧痛而战栗不已。

渐渐地，将所有的梦境场面（无论是欢乐还是痛苦）借由某种由水引发的灾难推向危机的顶峰，演变为了我梦境状态的习惯性倾向。在最初戒绝大麻后降临的状态中，我尤其曾因目睹人们跌落入矿井竖坑而惊恐万状，抑或正如我先前所详述的，因自己畏惧或承受某种坠入深渊的灾变而战栗；然而如今，无论我踏上何种旅途，是横渡大西洋抑或在内陆远足，迟早皆不可避免地以溺亡、抑或命悬一线的溺毙危机而告终。在清醒的状态下，有一点在我看来显得颇为奇异：在那些恐怖的噩梦之中，我竟然从未利用过往的经验来向自己确证某种危险不过是虚妄的假想。在放弃大麻之前，在自然的梦境中我曾经常运用逻辑推演的力量——对许多人而言，这种能力在睡眠中依然保持着相当程度的活跃——在心中对自己说道：“你先前就曾被这同一个危险吓到过，可结果证明它归根结底不过是意念的虚惊一场；”话音刚落，我便会立即苏醒过来，抑或目睹危险烟消云散。

意识到这一事实后，在白昼里我常常下定决心，要借由同样的权宜手段将自己从黑夜的痛苦折磨中唤醒；然而一旦噩梦降临，这一念头却连一次都未曾闪现过。大麻运作中那种将所有存在皆消融于当下、且对曾在前一种状态中生活过毫无记忆的规律法则，在戒断期沉睡的恐怖之中得到了最严丝合缝的彻底遵从。世间连一丝打破现实魔咒的可能猜想都未曾有过，而白昼的决意被彻底抛诸脑后，唯一的解救之道唯有咬牙坚忍到底。

然而，却曾有过一场最为痛彻心扉的异象，其终局证明乃是跳脱寻常恶水灾祸的例外，且以一种甚至在我完全清醒之后的数日里依然挥之不去的鲜活印记，深深烙印在我的脑海之中。正如其余诸多梦境一样，它亦同尼亚加拉紧密相连。

===Original===

On a cliff below the Fall, elevated to a height above the water such as only hashish can give, I found myself seated upon a broad flat stone.
Beside me, and resting her hand upon my own, sat a person whom I well knew, when awake, as a queenly woman of the world, who caressed society and was caressed by it in return. So far as man has a right to weigh his neighbor in the scales of private judgment, she was utterly hollow, selfish, and politic almost for policy’s sake. Indeed, I felt this, when awake, acting so powerfully as a repulsion, that had I, in actual life, found her so near me, I should have arisen and walked away for fear of showing her my true dislike. Now, however, I did not stir, for a singular fascination held me.
Presently she spoke, and called my attention to some object which was going down the river. I turned to look, but almost immediately heard a grinding sound beneath me, and felt the stone on which I sat slowly sliding toward the edge of the cliff. Facing about in an instant, I saw the woman gazing earnestly in another direction. Soon again she called me to look at some appearance in the river. Strangely reckless, I obeyed her. The stone slipped forward once more. This time I turned quick enough to detect her hand just moving away from the side. I sprang up; I caught her by the arm; I glared into her beautiful icy eyes; I cried out, “Woman! accursed woman! is this your faith?” Now, casting off all disguise, she gave a hollow laugh, and spoke: “Faith! do you look for faith in hell? I would have cast you to the fishes.” My eyes were opened. She said truly. We were indeed in hell, and I had not known it until now. Wearing the same features, with the demoniac instead of the human soul speaking through them﻿—wandering about the same earth, yet aware of no presence but demons like ourselves﻿—lit by the same sky, but hope spoke down from it no more.
I left the she-fiend by the river-brink, and met another as well known to me in the former life. Blandly she wound to my side as if she would entrap me, thinking that I was a newcomer into hell. Knowing her treachery, as if to embrace her I caught her in my arms, and, knitting them about her, strove to crush her out of being. With a look of awful malignity, she loosed one hand, and, tearing open her bosom, disclosed her heart, hissing hot, and pressed it upon my own.

===Chinese===

在瀑布下方的一处绝壁之上，耸立于唯有大麻方能赋予的高耸绝顶之水面上空，我发现自己端坐在一块宽大平整的石块上。

坐在我身旁、且将她的一只手搭在我手背上的，是一位在清醒时我深知其底细的世俗名媛，一位宛若女王般的女子，她宠幸着上流社交界，亦反过来备受其宠幸。纯就凡人有权在私下评判的天平上去衡量同胞的限度而言，她彻头彻尾空虚冷酷、自私自利，近乎是为了玩弄权术而玩弄权术。事实上，在清醒时我感受到这种特质作为一股强烈的排斥力在发挥作用，以至于在现实生活中倘若发现她离我如此之近，我定会因生怕流露出对她的真实厌恶而起身拂袖而去。然而在此刻，我却纹丝不动，因为一种奇异的魔力死死禁锢着我。

不多时她开口说话，唤我留意某件正顺流而下的漂浮物。我转头望去，但几乎在同一瞬间听闻脚下传来一阵刺耳的磨石摩擦声，感受到我所端坐的石块正缓缓向着绝壁边缘滑落。我猛然扭过头去，见那女子正全神贯注地凝视着另一个方向。很快她又再次唤我去瞧河里的某种景象。诡异而鲁莽地，我再次听从了她的话。石块再度向前滑移了一截。这一次我转身足够迅捷，恰好瞥见她的手正从石块侧面抽回。我一跃而起；一把死死扣住她的手臂；我狠狠瞪视着她那双美丽而冰冷的眼眸；我厉声喝道：“女人！遭诅咒的女人！这便是你的信义吗？”此时此刻，撕去了一切伪装，她发出一阵空洞阴冷的狞笑，开口说道：“信义？难道你在地狱里寻找信义吗？我原本正打算把你扔下去喂鱼呢。”我的双眼被彻底擦亮了。她言之凿凿。我们确确实实正置身于地狱之中，而我直到此刻方才如梦初醒。顶着同样的五官面貌，皮囊之下吐露言语的却是恶魔而非人类的灵魂——游荡漫步在同一个大地之上，却察觉不到除我们这般群魔之外的任何存在——被同一片苍穹所照亮，但希望却永不再自九天之上倾泻而下。

我将那女魔头弃置在河畔，迎面撞见了另一位在昔日生活中同样为我所深知的女子。她和颜悦色地凑到我身侧，仿佛企图将我诱入罗网，满以为我是初来乍到地狱的新客。深知其诡谲阴毒的背叛本性，我仿佛要将她拥入怀中般一把将其搂在臂弯之中，随后收紧双臂死死缠绞，极力试图将她从存在之中碾压成齑粉。带着一抹骇人听闻的怨毒狞笑，她抽出一只手，一把撕开自己的胸膛，露出了她那滚烫得咝咝煎熬作响的心脏，并狠狠按在了我自己的心脏之上。

===Original===

“The seal of love I bear thee, my chosen fiend!” she cried. Beneath that flaming signet my heart caught fire; I dashed her away, and then, thank God, awoke.

===Chinese===

“这便是我赋予你的爱的印记，我选中的恶魔！”她凄厉尖叫道。在那枚熊熊燃烧的火热印鉴之下，我的心脏瞬间烈火焚身；我一把将她狠狠推开，随后，感谢上帝，我终于惊醒了过来。

## XXIV The Visionary; To Which Chapter There Is No Admittance Upon Business / 第二十四章 幻视者：凡涉公事概莫入内
===Original===

There are those philosophers who, in running the boundary-line between the healthful and depraved propensities of our nature, have left the longing for stimulus on the condemned side. Notwithstanding all that I have suffered from the most powerful stimulant that the world possesses, I can not bring myself to agree with them. Not because the propensity is defensible on the ground of being universal. True, the Syrian has his hashish, the Chinaman his opium; he must be a poverty-stricken Siberian who lacks his ball of narcotic fungus, an impossible American who goes without tobacco, and over all the world liquor travels and domesticates itself, being of all stimulants the most thoroughly cosmopolitan. Yet, if we make this fact our basis, we are equally committed to the defense of the quite as catholic propensities toward lying, swearing, and hating one’s rival.
But there is one ground upon which the righteousness of the tendency toward stimulants may be upheld without the fear of any dangerous side-issues, namely, the fact that it proves, almost as powerfully as anything lower than direct revelation, man’s fitness by constitution and destiny by choice, for a higher set of circumstances than the present. Let it, however, be understood what, in this instance, is meant by the tendency to stimulus.
We do not mean that mere bodily craving which, shared equally in common by the most bestial and the most spiritual of men not disembodied, urges them alike to some expedient which will send their blood throbbing with a livelier thrill of physical well-being, blind them to the consideration of disagreeable truths, and eclipse all thought by the dense shadow of the Animal.
That of which we speak is something far higher﻿—the perception of the soul’s capacity for a broader being, deeper insight, grander views of Beauty, Truth, and Good than she now gains through the chinks of her cell. It is true that there are not many stimuli which possess the power in any degree to satisfy such yearnings. The whole catalogue, so far as research has written it, will probably embrace only opium, hashish, and, acting upon some rarely-found combinations of temperament, liquors.

===Chinese===

世间有些哲学家在为我们本性的健康倾向与堕落癖好划定界线之时，将对刺激物的渴望划归在了应受谴责的一侧。尽管我曾饱受世间最强效刺激物的残酷折磨，我却无法说服自己赞同他们的论断。这并非因为这种癖好凭借其普世普遍性便足以自圆其说。诚然，叙利亚人拥有他的大麻，中国人拥有他的鸦片；缺少了麻醉真菌球的定是穷困潦倒的西伯利亚人，而不沾烟草的几乎不可能算作地道的美国人，至于烈酒更是在全世界四处游历、安家落户，在所有刺激物中称得上最彻头彻尾的世界主义者。然而，倘若我们将这一事实作为立论基石，我们便同样有义务为贪婪说谎、诅咒发誓与嫉恨仇敌这些同样普世的恶劣倾向予以辩护。

然而，却存在着另一重足以捍卫趋向刺激物之倾向的合理性、且无须担忧引来任何危险副效应的坚实依据——那便是：它以仅次于直接神圣启示的强大力量，几乎毫无置疑地证明了：人类凭其体质构造与凭其抉择注定的宿命，皆天然契合于某种远比当下更为崇高的环境际遇。然而，在此处必须先行厘清：所谓对刺激物的倾向究竟所指为何。

我们所指的绝非单纯的肉体渴望——那种凡未脱离肉胎的凡人（无论是最兽性还是最富灵性之人）所普遍共享的渴求，促使他们同样去寻觅某种能令血液伴随着更欢腾的肉体快感而剧烈跳动、使其对令人不快的真理视若无睹、并借由“兽性”的浓重阴影遮蔽一切思想的权宜手段。

我们所言及的乃是某种远为崇高之物——即灵魂对更辽阔的存在、更深邃的洞见、以及对“美、真、善”之宏伟视野的感知能力，这些崇高之物乃是她如今透过牢房的狭隘缝隙所无法企及的。诚然，世间并不存在太多有能力在任何程度上满足这般渴慕的刺激物。就目前的探索记载所列出的全部目录而言，恐怕仅涵盖了鸦片、大麻、以及在某些罕见的特定体质组合下发挥效力的烈酒。

===Original===

Ether, chloroform, and the exhilarant gases may be left out of the consideration, since but a few people are enthusiastic or reckless enough in the pursuit of remarkable emotions to tamper with agents so evanescent in their immediate, so fatal in their prolonged effects.
But, wherever the yearning of the mind is toward gratifications of this nature﻿—where it is calling earnestly for a nobler excellence in all its objects, nay, even wearied, discontented with those it now has, shall we pronounce this state a right or a wrong one?
Let us see what verdict we would give upon certain other yearnings. When the poor man fences in for himself a little spot of waste land, he first erects a dark and low cabin, that his household gods may not be shelterless. Pleased for a while by the hovel life, greatly better as it is than camping out upon the roofless moor, he feels all his aim satisfied, and insphered within the attainment of Nature’s response to sheer physical necessities.
By-and-by, after the pleasures of not being cold, wet, exposed to suspicious eyesight, nor hungry (since he has a little potato-patch behind the cabin), have become somewhat odd to him, he happens to think, “How would a few flowers look before my door? There is something inside of me which seems to approve of flowers; I think they would do me good.” So the poor man wanders out into the wood, and there, in that most ancient and incense-breathing temple of our God, he kneels down on the turfy hassock, which Spring, that ever-young opener of the cathedral doors, has laid for him, and gently, without unearthing a fibre of their roots, lifts a clump of violets.
When, a day or two afterward, we come along past the rude cabin, and as we lean over the fence to ask the tenant how he fares, what do we do when our eyes fall upon a little dot here and there of something more than ground, or grass, or vegetables﻿—azure faces looking brotherly up at the same-colored heaven? Do we shake our heads, draw down our brows, purse our mouths, and say, “Ah! dissatisfied with your circumstances, I see. Restless where Providence has placed you; grasping after visionary happiness; morbidly craving for what you have not; depraved taste!” and all that sort of thing?

===Chinese===

乙醚、氯仿以及令人兴奋的笑气大可排除在考量之外，因为极少有人在追寻非凡情感的狂热或鲁莽中，敢于去招惹这类在即刻效应上转瞬即逝、在长远后果中却致命致命的烈性媒介。

然而，凡心智的渴求乃是朝向此类性质的心灵满足之处——凡它在迫切呼唤其所有对象展现出更高尚卓越之美、甚至对自己当下所拥有的一切感到厌倦不满之处，我们难道当判定这种状态乃是错误邪僻的吗？

且让我们瞧瞧对于其他某些渴慕追求我们会作出何种裁决。当一个穷汉为自己圈占下一小块荒地之时，他首先搭建起一座昏暗低矮的棚屋，免得自家的庇护神灵无处容身。因栖身于茅舍生活而欣喜了片刻——毕竟这远胜于露宿在没有屋顶的荒原旷野之中——他感到自己的全部目标皆已达成，安然自得于大自然对纯粹生理必需的供给之内。

久而久之，在不再受冻、挨淋、免遭猜忌目光窥探、以及不再忍饥挨饿（因为他在茅舍后开辟了一小块土豆地）的快乐逐渐变得习以为常之后，他偶然间心想：“倘若在我的门前栽上几朵鲜花，那该是何等模样？我的内心深处似乎对鲜花心怀赞许；依我看它们定会让我心情舒畅。”于是这位穷汉漫步走入树林，在那座属于我们上帝的最古老且散发着馨香的圣殿之中，他跪倒在春姑娘——那位大教堂殿门永远年轻的开启者——为他铺设的草皮蒲团上，轻柔地、未曾刨断半根根须地，挖起了一丛紫罗兰。

一两天后，当我们从这座粗陋的木屋旁经过，倚在篱笆上探问住户境况如何时，当我们眼见地上此处彼处点缀着些许超越了泥土、杂草或蔬菜的崭新微芒——一朵朵蔚蓝的面庞宛若同胞手足般仰望同色的苍穹时，我们究竟会作何反应？难道我们会连连摇头，深锁双眉，撇起嘴唇斥责道：“哈！看样子是对自己的处境心怀不满呢。不安分守己安于天命的安排；妄图攫取虚妄的幸福；病态地贪求自己原本没有的事物；真是不知天高地厚的堕落品味！”诸如此类的话吗？

===Original===

I had really flattered myself that I was going to make a pretty cogent combination out of this, of the *a fortiori* and *reductio ad absurdum* arguments, but I am afraid I have failed. I fear that there are some people who *would* say exactly this.
Yet I will restrict the “we” to you and to myself, my reader, since I know that you have not the ability nor I the will to be guilty of so gross a speech. *We*, then, certainly shall not say it.
Let us finish the analogy. A man who, during his childish (not his childlike) years, was growing up into all that compacts, rounds out, and confirms the animal, has in that time attended solely to those claims of nature which have a reference to assimilation and secretion. With meat, drink, and raiment he was satisfied. Practical men cherished him as a sort of typical fact of that other broader fact, the respectable community.
Just at the moment that hopes of his “making something of himself” are at their widest (I will not say “highest,” since there is no height to hopes of this kind, as ordinarily understood), he discovers that he has some other need hitherto unsuspected, and not coming under any caption in the catalogue of bodily well-being. His soul wants beauty; its yearning will not be repressed. For a while he is content with the discovery of that which springs up between his feet in this really very beautiful world. Absorbed in other aims, he had never noticed it before, and now it breaks upon him as from a new heaven and a new earth.
By-and-by he thinks that, since all this loveliness is transitory, liable to be obscured by clouds and bedraggled by storms, uprooted utterly or made distasteful by the presence of a bad association which will not be exiled, his soul, as immortal and expansive, may find grander views in another region.
This other is the region of stimulus. What shall we say to this man? “You are morbid; you are depraved; your yearnings are unnatural and sinful; you must contract your wishes, or, at least, extend your arms sideways farther into the dark, not upward higher into the light?”
No; a thousand times no! Let us rather say thus: “Man, in this your longing, you have the noblest testimony to the endless capacity for growth of that germ, your soul.

===Chinese===

我原本确实自负地以为，自己将由此推演出一套将举轻明重（*a fortiori*）与归谬法（*reductio ad absurdum*）极其有力融会贯通的雄辩论证，可我恐怕自己失算了。我深恐世间确凿有些人*当真*会说出这般言辞。

然而，读者诸君，我愿将这“我们”限定在你我二人之间，因为我深知你既无能力、我亦无意愿犯下如此粗鄙无理的狂言。那么，*我们*自然绝不会吐露此等恶语。

让我们把这一类比贯彻到底。一个人，倘若在其幼童（而非童真）岁月里，终日致力于充实、丰满并巩固其兽性本能，在整段岁月里唯独专注于那些关乎消化吸收与排泄分泌的生理机能。有酒肉果腹、有衣衫裹体，他便心满意足。务实的人们将他视作典范，视作那个更为宏大的群体——“体面阶层”——的微观缩影予以悉心呵护。

正当人们对他“日后必成大器”的期许达到最宽广（我不会说“最高”，因为通常理解的此类期许根本毫无高度可言）的巅峰关头，他突然发现自己体内涌现出了某种此前从未被怀疑过的崭新匮乏——且绝无法归入肉体福祉清单的任何纲目之下。他的灵魂渴求着美丽；这股渴望再也无法被强行扼杀。有一阵子，在这座确实美轮美奂的世界里，他对发现那些从自己脚边破土而出的寻常景致感到心满意足。以往沉溺于其他务实目标，他从未留意过它们，如今它们向他扑面而来，宛若展现自一片崭新的诸天与崭新的大地。

久而久之他思索道：既然所有这些可爱之物皆转瞬即逝，极易被乌云遮蔽、被风暴摧残，抑或因某种无法驱逐的恶劣联想而彻底连根拔起乃至变得令人作呕，那么他的灵魂，作为不朽且不断拓展的存在，或许能在另一片领域中觅得更为崇高壮阔的视野。

这另一片天地，便是刺激物的领域。对此人我们究竟该作何评论？“你病态了；你堕落了；你的渴慕乃是非自然且充满罪恶的；你必须收敛你的非分之想，或者至少把双臂往两侧更深地伸入黑暗，而绝不可向上更高地伸向光明？”

不；一千个不！我们倒当如是相告：“凡人啊，在你这番向往之中，蕴含着对你灵魂那粒灵性胚芽具有无尽生长潜能的最崇高见证。

===Original===

You can not believe more of her than she is, for you can not believe more of her than God believes, and He was assured that He had made her in His own image. You do not, therefore, flatter yourself with the privilege of looking into things too high for you; there is nothing which you can conceive of as possible to your view which shall not be actual. Your wish is approved by Heaven, for from Heaven came the constitution which made you capable of such a wish. Your Creator does not condemn you, neither do we condemn you.”
If that man therefore departs, and becomes addicted to the indulgence in opium, hashish, or whatever other spell may in his case possess the power of prying open for him the gates to more wondrous glories, shall not the blood of the man and the tears of ruined or bitterly sympathizing friends be upon our skirts?
Nay, most just and noble-hearted reader, for that which we have said to him should be only the exordium to another, a longer address. It is not the author’s will more than his province to be dictative, yet be indulgent if he shortly sketches it here.
“You sin not in your yearnings. Yet may you sin grievously, even against the grand aim of those yearnings, by a certain suicidal gratification of them. Were hashish, or opium, or aught else of kindred nature between the poles the only alternative to your former gross life in mere meat and drink, the only alternative even to remaining within the limits of your first acquired beauty, it were better indeed to use them than to dishonor your soul by following mere material aims, or by crippling her energies of expansion.
“Yet this is not the alternative. In Nature there is yet undiscovered glory, a spirit which gradually will interpenetrate you as you commune with her. She is not a mockery, a sham, for a truthful essence indwells, informs her. Be this communing one stimulus to you!
“In Art there is also a spirit which you have not yet read. As the spirit of Nature is the ideal of God, so is the spirit of Art the ideal of man, the mind which God has made. With this also commune. In your actions upon it, in its reactions upon you, you will rejoice in perceptions of a meaning in life which you never felt; you will have one more stimulus.

===Chinese===

你对她的赞誉绝不可能超出她的本真，因为你对她的信念绝无法超越上帝对她的笃信，而上帝深知自己是依照自身的形象创造了她。因此，你绝非自视过高妄图窥探非分之境；凡你所能构想为视界可能之物，皆无一不可化为现实。你的愿景蒙受苍天的赞许，因为赋予你产生此等愿景禀赋的体质正是出自苍天的恩赐。你的造物主未曾定你的罪，我们亦不曾定你的罪。”

倘若此人因此转身离去，沉溺于对鸦片、大麻、或任何在他身上有能力撬开通往更为神奇荣耀之门的其他魔咒之中，那么此人的鲜血、以及其身败名裂或痛心共鸣的朋友们的泪水，岂非要算在我们袍服的血债之上吗？

绝非如此，最公允且高尚的读者啊，因为我们方才向他吐露之语，不过是另一篇更漫长演说的开场序曲罢了。作者绝无意愿、亦无资格在此颐指气使发号施令，然而倘若他在此将其简明勾勒一番，尚祈读者宽容包涵：

“你的渴慕并无过错。然而若采取某种自杀式的纵欲来满足它们，你便可能铸成大错，甚至彻底违背那些渴慕的崇高初衷。倘若在这天地两极之间，大麻、鸦片或任何同类之物乃是摆脱以往单纯酒肉凡俗生活的唯一抉择，乃是超越你最初习得之有限美感的唯一出路，那么去享用它们确实胜过追逐单纯物质目标以玷污你的灵魂、抑或扼杀她拓展升华的蓬勃生机。

“然而，这绝非唯一的抉择。在大自然之中，尚有未经发掘的万道荣光，一股当你同她交融神会时定会逐步将你洞穿渗透的神圣灵性。她绝非嘲弄，绝非虚饰，因为一种真实的本质深藏其间、赋予其生机。且让这番交融神会成为激励你的一剂刺激吧！

“在艺术之中，亦有一股你尚未破译的灵性。正如大自然的灵性乃是上帝的崇高理想一样，艺术的灵性正是人类——上帝所塑造之心智——的至高理想。且同这股灵性交融神会吧。在你对它的施加之中，在它对你的反冲塑造之中，你将为你此前从未体会到的生命真谛而欣喜若狂；你将拥有又一剂崇高的刺激。

===Original===

“Around you are the starving to be fed, the naked to be clothed, the captive to be set free, the persecuted to be overshadowed by your wing, the benighted to be enlightened, the vile to be cleansed. Do good as you have opportunity, and find one more stimulus in that.
“The Infinite One is communing with this illimitable soul of yours to lift it higher. At a hundred doors he comes in to you continually. There are breathings within you which are not of yourself. Do you find yourself lower than you would be? Straightway the standard of true height is shown to you, held in a hand which can help you up to it. Are you obscured by the shadow of a misused past? To you, when you muse in the twilight, come angels, like the two who came even to Sodom at evening. There is hope of a better growth, a grander life; the light of a resurrection which shoots in from the time to come through the chinks of that sepulchre, your body. Wait patiently﻿—ah! for how few moments, and that sepulchre shall be a cenotaph. Let that hope of an advancing future, with all its unveiling of mysteries, its impulse along the path of an ever more and more glorious career, its exhaustless Beauty, and Truth, and Good, be your last, your noblest, your unfailing stimulus, until the Ideal and the Actual become the same, and it be needed no longer.
“But of the stimulus of drugs, of potions, beware. For the sake of that very majesty with which you justly wish to aggrandize your soul, beware. Their fountains will be presently exhausted, and then you shall helplessly beat your breast, as without possibility of arising from the brink you draw in their foul, their maddening lees, and curse yourself for slaying those noble powers which it was your longing to strengthen, to nourish, and to clarify.”
Let this illustration be pardoned if, in spite of other intentions, it has become a sermon. The hashish-eater knows full well that not only in the world, but in our own country, shamelessly vilified as it is by the ignorant of other lands with the opprobrium of an all-absorbing aim at gain, there are many of those spirits who can not steep themselves in oblivion of all but physical ends, who can not rest in the mere knowledge that they are getting so many houses, so many acres of land, so much respectable consideration, to be possessed while a wind is passing by, while a twilight is fading.

===Chinese===

“在你身旁，正有饥馁者等待果腹，赤身者等待衣裳，身陷囹圄者等待解放，受迫害者等待你羽翼的庇佑，蒙昧愚顽者等待启蒙教化，鄙陋沉沦者等待洗涤救赎。但行善事莫问前程，且在其中寻得又一剂崇高的刺激。

“那位‘无限者’正同你这无边无际的灵魂交融神会，意在将它超拔至更高境界。穿透百道门扉，祂正持续不断地步入你的心扉。在你体内正有并非出自你自身的深沉吐息。你发觉自己远低于期许的境界吗？真正崇高的尺度刹那间便展示在你眼前，高擎在能助你登临的高耸巨手之中。你被虚掷过往的阴霾所蒙蔽遮掩吗？每当你在薄暮中沉思冥想之际，天使便降临于你身侧，正宛若当年在傍晚踏入所多玛（Sodom）的那两位天使一样。世间正有通往更好成长、更宏大生命的希望；一场复活的光辉正自未来的岁月穿透这具作为你坟冢的肉体缝隙直射而入。耐心静候吧——啊！只需短短片刻，那座坟冢便将化作一座空冢（cenotaph）。愿那对步步逼近之未来的希望——连同其揭示的一切奥秘、其在日益辉煌宏大航程中所注入的无穷动力、其取之不尽的‘美、真、善’——成为你最终、最崇高、永不枯竭的生命刺激，直至‘理想’与‘现实’彻底融为一体，且刺激物再无存在的必要。

“然而面对药物与药剂的刺激，请务必当心。看在正合情理地渴望升华灵魂的无上威严分上，当心啊。它们的泉眼转瞬即逝便将枯竭殆尽，届时你定会无助地捶胸顿足，因为在根本毫无可能从悬崖边缘脱身自拔的绝境中，你将不得不吞咽下它们污秽肮脏、令人发狂的残渣沉淀，并为自己亲手扼杀了原本渴望壮大、滋养与澄清的那些崇高力量而懊悔诅咒自己。”

倘若这一阐发在违背原本初衷的情况下不幸演变成了一篇布道说教，尚祈读者见谅。食大麻者深知：不仅在全世界，而且在我们自己的国度里——尽管它被其他国度无知之徒肆意用“一心唯利是图”的恶谥进行无耻攻讦诋毁——依然存在着诸多绝不肯将自己浸泡沉沦在除物质目标之外一切皆遭遗忘的深渊之中的高尚灵魂，他们绝无法仅仅满足于得知自己正在不断斩获这许多栋豪宅、这许多英亩田产、这许多受人体面的虚名声望——这些事物不过宛如一阵穿堂风掠过、一片暮色消逝般转瞬即逝。

===Original===

There are men who pine restlessly for riches which shall satisfy higher obligations of their being, shall endure longer, shall in themselves possess a nobler and more expansive essence. They are right in this pining. Yet if there be one voice which can speak from the gateway of a dangerous avenue to its satisfaction, that can say, “Ho there! pass by; I have tried this way; it leads at last into poisonous wildernesses,” in the name of Heaven let it be raised.
And thus I excuse my sermon.
There are those, no doubt, who in reading it will say, “Is it not inconsistent to advise this possible hashish-eater to ‘feed the hungry and clothe the naked’ after inveighing so much against practical aims just before?” With a desire to anticipate this objection, I would here say that it is not against practical aims, but the making them the chief, the controlling ones. Or, rather, even more boldly, not against practical aims at all, but against pseudo-practical. Paradoxical as it may be, there is no man more thoroughly, more purely practical than he who is most truly ideal. It is needless to suggest that the word “practical” is a derivative from the Greek verb “to do,” and is therefore most properly applied to the man who “does” the best for himself. Now which of two beings thus does the best for himself, he who does it particularly for that part of him which, in a few days, he is to abandon forever, or he who does it to the part which is eternally to abide by him? O practical men, judge ye.
The most perfect spirituality of aim, moreover, is not violated by any decent and orderly attention to the claims of the body. Only let the house be not more beautified than the tenant, the servant fed and adorned above his master, and then no one in his senses can quarrel because either the servant or the house is well sustained for the master’s highest good.
It is, no doubt, the perversion of this principle which has caused the word “visionary,” most righteously belonging, by its first title, to souls of the grandest insight, to be held, together with the idea which it conveys, in contempt even by serious and thoughtful men.

===Chinese===

世间确有些人，正焦灼不宁地向往着那些能满足其存在之更高使命、能历经更久岁月磨砺、其自身蕴含着更高尚更具拓展性本质的真正财富。在这一焦灼渴求中，他们无可指摘。然而，倘若有一道声音能够伫立在通往这种满足的危险歧途关隘前高声断喝：“止步！从旁绕行；我曾试过这条歧路；它最终通往致命的毒草荒原，”那么看在苍天的分上，请让这道声音响彻天际吧。

我由此为自己的这番布道寻找到了合理解释。

毫无疑问有人在读到此处时会质问：“刚刚才对务实的目标痛加抨击，紧接着便建议这位潜在的食大麻者去‘饥者食之，寒者衣之’，这难道不是自相矛盾吗？”为了预先回应这一异议，我在此必须指出：我所反对的并非务实的目标本身，而是反对将其奉为首要的、支配性的主宰法则。或者更为大胆地说，我反对的根本不是务实的目标，而是反对伪务实的目标。无论听起来多么不可思议，世间没有任何人能比最真正的理想主义者更为彻底、更为纯粹地务实。无须提醒人们：“务实”（practical）一词源于希腊语动词“行”（to do，*πράττειν*），因此最合宜地应当运用于为自己“行”求最佳福祉之人。那么在这两类人中，究竟是谁在为自己谋求最佳福祉呢？是那个专门为自己在短短数日后便将永远抛弃的一部分躯壳操劳谋利之人，还是那个全心为了将永恒同他相依相伴的核心灵魂谋求至福之人？啊，务实的人们啊，请你们自己裁决吧。

更有甚者，对肉体需求的合情合理、井然有序的关照，丝毫未曾违背目标的最完满属灵神圣性。只要这栋寓所的装潢修饰不至于凌驾于住客之上，只要仆役的饮食穿戴不至于越俎代庖超越他的主人，那么世间凡具神志清醒理智之人，绝无一人会因为仆役或寓所为了主人的至高福祉而获得优渥滋养保障而大发雷霆。

毫无疑问，正是对这一原则的曲解歪曲，导致了“幻视者”（visionary）这个按其最初头衔本属于拥有最宏大洞察力之崇高灵魂的字眼，连同其所承载的崇高理念，甚至在严肃深沉的思想家眼中亦沦为了饱受轻蔑的贬义词。

===Original===

Shallow persons, urging that claim to notoriety through extravagance, which they were aware they could not press to celebrity by greatness, have been disgusting humanity with their absurdities from the time that Diogenes coiled himself in his tub down to the era of the last apostle who blew his trumpet through Broadway. They have all glorified themselves with the name “visionary;” when the radiant mantle fell from the shoulders of the last ascending prophet who had worn it in reverence, it was snatched by the ancestor of all the unseemly clan﻿—it cloaked the rags of his spiritual beggary during his lifetime, and at his decession it was handed down through every succeeding generation of impostors. No better proof could be adduced for its primeval authentic dignity than the fact that there has never, within the memory of man, been a pseudo-poet, pseudo-philanthropist, or a pseudo- with any other termination, who has not tenaciously clung to the epithet as his birthright, his mark of the elect, his cross of the Legion of Honor.
We can not wonder at the astonishment expressed by Rogers, that most substantial banker of a most substantial country, when, after Byron had dined with him, for the sake of the spiritual man, upon one potato and a glass of water, refusing all the English cheer set prodigally before him, the moneyed man finds that, within the next hour, his brother bard has dispatched a steak and a bottle of Port at his clubhouse!
Yet this assumption of the spiritual where it does not exist﻿—this counterfeit presentment of the true visionary, certainly ought not, among thinking men at least, to discredit the real fact.
There are, doubtless, more than one who, when they have heard this fine word rung mournfully from some old watchtower of conventional respectability, as the knell of all confidence, all position, all esteem among men, or echoing portentously from the tripod of Sir Oracle, big with evil omen to an unendorsed theory, have sighed for the ancient days when it beautified the threnody over a dead seer, or pealed from the lips of harpers as they sang the forecast of a living sage.

===Chinese===

浅薄虚妄之辈，由于自知无法凭借伟大跻身显赫之列，便极力借由荒诞放浪来博取臭名昭著的声望；从第欧根尼（Diogenes）蜷缩在木桶之中的时代开始，直至在百老汇沿街吹响号角的最后一位自封使徒的年代，他们始终以其荒诞不经的怪癖行径令全人类为之作呕。他们皆以“幻视者”之名为自己脸上贴金；当那袭璀璨的光明斗篷从最后一位曾虔敬披戴它的升天先知肩头滑落之际，便被这群不成体统的丑类的始祖一把攫取——在生前它遮掩了他属灵乞丐般的破衣烂衫，而在他寿终正寝之际，它又被代代相传遗留给了每一代的骗子行伍。对于其太初正统尊严最好的明证莫过于：在人类的记忆之中，世间从未有过任何一位伪诗人、伪慈善家、或任何以“伪”字打头的跳梁小丑，未曾顽固地将这一绰号紧紧攥在掌心，视作其与生俱来的权利、天选之民的徽记、以及法国荣誉军团的十字勋章。

我们丝毫不必对罗杰斯（Rogers）——那个最讲究实际之国度里最殷实稳健的银行家——所展现出的错愕震惊感到诧异：当年拜伦（Byron）与他共进晚餐时，为了彰显属灵高士的超脱凡俗，断然拒绝了摆在他面前丰盛奢华的全套英式珍馐，仅仅用一个土豆和一杯水草草果腹；然而不出一个钟头，这位有钱的绅士便发觉他那位诗人同行正在自己的俱乐部会所里狼吞虎咽地干掉了一块厚牛排和一整瓶波特酒！

然而，在毫无灵性之处对属灵境界的这番僭越假冒——对真正“幻视者”的这番伪劣仿冒，在有深度的思想家眼中，绝不应当让客观真实的事实本身蒙受污名。

毫无疑问，不仅有一人，每当听闻这一华美典雅的词汇从某些墨守成规之体面传统的古老瞭望塔上被凄然敲响、宛若向世间的一切信任、地位与尊崇鸣响丧钟；抑或从神谕先生（Sir Oracle）的三脚祭坛上投射出凶兆的回响、向某种未经背书的学说降下灾难预兆之时，皆会忍不住长叹怀念起昔日的古老岁月——在那些岁月里，它曾点亮过悼念已故先知的挽歌神采，抑或从行吟弹琴乐师的双唇间破晓而出，高唱出赞颂在世圣哲的预言乐章。

===Original===

To its old place the “visionary” will never be restored until knaves cease to make it their claim to spurious reverence, or good men refrain from looking at every theory as unsafe which does not base its request for their attention upon some tendency to promote a bodily good or explain a bodily fact. If the former can not, is it possible that the latter may not be?
For him who shall reinstate that word there is a noble meed waiting in the future. The man who leaps into a stream and brings his drowning brother safe to shore is rewarded by the Humane Society with a medal, which he is proud to hand down to his children as their best inheritance. If we are true men, Truth is brother to us all, and the representative of a great and good idea is Truth. Help! then, help! until someone comes who shall place in the reverence of just thinkers. Verily he shall not lose his reward. But he must be a man of calm nerve as well as bold stroke; as able to take full in his face the outrageous pelting of the spray, as to wear the medal when he has wiped off the drops.
Then shall the soul be held worthier than the body, not only in-, but outside of the pale of speculative theology, and

> “Then comes the statelier Eden back to man;

Then springs the crowning race of human kind.

May these things be!”

===Chinese===

除非那些市井无赖不再将其据为己有作为索取虚妄敬意的护身符，除非正人君子不再将凡未能以促进肉体福祉或阐释肉体表象为其诉求的一切理论皆视作危险异端，“幻视者”这一词汇便绝无可能重返其昔日的崇高宝座。倘若前者尚无可能，难道后者亦绝无实现的希望吗？

对于凡能使这一词汇重获荣光之人，在未来正有一笔高贵的赏赐在静候着他。跳入湍急河流将溺水同胞安全营救上岸之人，必将蒙受人道协会（Humane Society）颁发的一枚勋章，他以此为荣并将其视作传给子孙的最佳宝贵家产。倘若我们皆是堂堂正正的真正男儿，那么“真理”便是我们所有人的骨肉兄弟，而宏大崇高理念的化身正是“真理”。援手吧！那么，伸出援手吧！直到某位能将其重新置于公允思想家的敬重之席的人横空出世。诚然，他必不致失去他的赏赐。但他必须是一位兼具沉着坚韧神经与大刀阔斧魄力的勇士；不仅能迎面承受风暴巨浪的残酷肆虐抽打，更能在此后拭去水珠、骄傲佩戴胸前闪烁的勋章。

届时，灵魂必将超越肉体被奉为更为尊贵的存在——不仅在思辨神学的藩篱之内，亦在藩篱之外普照尘寰，并且

> “届时更为巍峨庄严的伊甸园必将重返人间；

届时人类至高无上的族类必将蓬勃萌发。

但愿这些宏愿终成现实！”

## XXV Cave Succedanea / 第二十五章 慎防替代物
===Original===

I am not aware of the existence of any in this part of the world who are now in the habit of using hashish. Those persons to whom, at their request, I formerly administered it, for experiment’s sake, were satisfied with the one trial, upon my assuring them that any prolonged indulgence would infallibly lead to horrors.
Yet, since it is not at all impossible that these pages may meet the eye of those who, unknown to me, are incipient hashish-eaters, or who, having tested to the full the powers of the drug, now find its influence a slavery, yet are ignorant of the proper means of emancipation, I will not let this opportunity pass for suggesting, through a somewhat further narrative of my own case, a counsel which may chance to be salutary.
The hashish-eater needs particularly to resist the temptation of retreating, in the trials of his slow disenthrallment, to some other stimulus, such as liquors or opium. Against such a retreat I was warned by the same adviser whose article in the Magazine had been my prime motor to escape.
As in an early part of this narrative it has been mentioned, strong experimental tendencies had led me, long before the first acquaintance with hashish, to investigate the effect of all narcotics and stimulants, not so much with a view to pleasure as to the discovery of new phases of mental life. Among these researches had been opium. This drug never affected me very powerfully, not in one instance producing anything like hallucination, but operating principally through a quiet which no external circumstances could disturb﻿—slightly tinged, when my eyes were shut, with pleasing images of scenery. Its mild effect was probably owing to some resistant peculiarity of constitution, since I remember having once taken a dose, which I afterward learned, upon good authority, to have been sufficient to kill three healthy men, without any remarkable phenomena ensuing. Several considerations operated with me to prevent my making opium an habitual indulgence, besides this fact of its moderate potency. This, of itself, might not have been sufficient, since the capability which I acquired in its use of sustaining the most prolonged and severe fatigue was in my case unexampled.
In the first place, I was secured from enslavement by the terrors of De Quincey’s suffering.

===Chinese===

在世界上的这一角落，就我所知，如今已再无人保留着服食大麻的习癖。那些以往曾出于好奇请求我向其提供药物以供实验的人们，在我向其确证任何长期的沉溺皆必将导致骇人恶果之后，皆满足于那仅仅一次的尝试。

然而，鉴于这些篇章绝非全无可能落入某些我不结识的初涉大麻者眼中，抑或某些已然充分领教了该药物伟力、如今发觉其影响已然化作沉重奴役、却苦于不知该如何正当脱身解脱之人的视野之中，我不愿错失这一契机，通过对自己病例的进一步记述，提出一番或许偶能发挥裨益的良善忠告。

食大麻者在艰难缓慢的解脱历程中，尤需竭力抵御退求其他刺激物（诸如烈酒或鸦片）作为替代慰藉的诱惑。针对这种倒退行径，那位曾在杂志上发表文章、成为我逃出生天的最初动力的良师益友，就曾向我敲响过警钟。

正如在本书前文中所提及的那样，早在我初次结识大麻之前，强烈的实验探索倾向便曾驱使我去探究一切麻醉剂与刺激物的效力，与其说是为了寻欢作乐，倒不如说是为了发掘心智生命的新领域。在这些探求之中，便涵盖了鸦片。这种药物从未对我产生过多么强烈的冲击，未曾有一次催生出哪怕一丝幻觉，而主要是通过一种任何外在环境皆无法扰乱的宁静发挥效力——每当闭合双目之际，仅隐约点缀着令人愉悦的风光意象。它那温和的效力或许归因于某种具有抵抗力的特殊生理体质，因为我清楚记得自己曾服下过一剂据可靠医学权威日后告知足以毒死三名壮汉的巨量，却未曾引发任何引人注目的异常现象。除了药效平平这一事实之外，尚有若干考量阻碍了我将鸦片演变为一种习惯性的嗜好。单凭药效温和本身或许尚不足以阻遏我，因为我在服用它时所获得的承受极度持久且繁重疲劳的能力，在我身上委实史无前例。

首先，德·昆西（De Quincey）所承受的恐怖痛苦，成为了阻止我沦为鸦片奴隶的有力防线。

===Original===

I felt assured that he had not unmasked the half of it, since his exquisite sense of the refined and the appropriate in all communion with the public, showing itself in a thousand places throughout his works, had evidently withheld him, in his confessions, from giving to the painful intaglio that deep stroke of the graver which he thought that good taste would not permit, even under sanction of truth.
Again, a consideration of more narrow prejudice withheld me﻿—the impossibility, if I should use opium, of concealing the fact from my associates, some of whom were physicians, and hardly any of them so unobserving as not to be attracted curiously to the peculiarities of the opium eye, complexion, and manner.
At this time the reputation of being an opium-eater was one very little desirable in the community which included me, had its further abominable consequences been recklessly put aside. It was impossible for anyone known to have used the drug to make any intellectual effort whatever, speech, published article, or brilliant conversation, without being hailed satirically as Coleridge *le petit*, or De Quincey in the second edition. That this was not altogether a morbid condition of public sentiment in the microcosm where I dwelt, may be inferred from a fact which, occurring a few months before I entered it, had no doubt acted to tinge general opinion.
A certain person, in reading “The Confessions,” had gathered from them (it would be hard to say how, since their author everywhere expresses the opium state as one whose serenity is repulsive to all action for the time being) that he should be able to excel De Quincey upon his own field if he wrote while at the height of the effect. Setting apart one evening for the English opium-eater’s literary discomfiture, he drank his laudanum, and locked himself into his room alone with the awful presence of a quire of foolscap. On the following morning, his friends, knocking at the door repeatedly, received no answer, and, fearful of some accident, broke in the lock. Lo! our De Quincey in petto was seated in his chair, with pen in hand, and his forehead resting on a blank mass of paper, in all the *abandon* of innocent repose!
After the final abandonment of hashish, however, at times, when distress had reduced me to the willingness to test any relief save that of return, I once or twice tried the effect of opium.

===Chinese===

我深信他向世人所揭露的苦难不及真实境况的一半，因为他在同公众的一切交流中对典雅得体与合宜分寸的敏锐拿捏——这在其整部著作的千百处细节中皆表露无遗——显然在他撰写忏悔录时，遏制住了他用雕刻刀在痛苦的凹版上刻下他认为即便在真理许可下优良品味亦绝不容忍的至深刀痕。

再者，一种出于更为狭隘的世俗顾虑亦遏制了我——倘若我服食鸦片，我将绝无可能向同伴们隐瞒这一事实，他们之中不乏医者，且几乎无一人会迟钝到对吸食鸦片特有的眼神、气色与神态产生不了探究的好奇心。

在彼时彼刻，在容纳我的这一社交圈子中，即便撇开其进一步的丑恶后果不谈，顶着“食鸦片者”的名号亦是极不光彩的。任何已知曾使用过该药物的人，无论做出何等智力上的努力——无论是演说、发表文章、还是口若悬河的高谈阔论——皆不可避免地会被人尖酸讽刺地冠以“小柯勒律治（Coleridge le petit）”、抑或“再版德·昆西”的谑称。这在我所栖身的小天地里绝非完全是一种病态的公众舆论，从我踏入该地数月前发生的一桩无疑对普遍舆论起到定调作用的轶事中，便可窥见一斑。

某人在研读《忏悔录》时，从中得出了一种结论（委实难以参透他究竟是如何得出这般结论的，因为其作者在字里行间皆将鸦片状态描摹为一种其恬静在当时对一切行动皆心生抗拒的境界）：倘若他在药效达到巅峰的时刻下笔写作，便必能在德·昆西自身的领域将其彻底超越。他专门拨出一个傍晚意图让这位英国食鸦片者在文坛颜面扫地，服下了他的劳丹宁（laudanum，鸦片酊），并将自己反锁在房间里，独自面对一整叠大张稿纸（foolscap）那可畏的临在。次日清晨，他的朋友们反复敲门却未得应答，生怕发生意外便破锁而入。看哪！我们这位暗自自命不凡的小德·昆西（De Quincey in petto）正端坐在扶手椅上，手中握着墨水笔，额头正枕在一大堆白纸之上，完全沉浸在无辜安歇的安然酣睡（*abandon*）之中！

然而，在彻底戒除大麻之后，偶尔在极度痛苦驱使我愿意尝试除重蹈覆辙之外的任何解脱手段时，我曾有一两次尝试过鸦片的效力。

===Original===

It was invariably bad, not operating, as a renewal of the hashish indulgence would have done, to lift me into the former plane of pleasurable activity and interest in things about me, but singularly combining with whatever of the hashish force might be remaining in my system to cover me once more with the pall which made the worst parts of the old life so painful. Insane faces glared at me; dire voices of prophecy spoke to me even when wide awake; I was filled with foreboding of some impending wrathful visitation, and learned to my sorrow that I was only exchanging one bitter cup for another. As the opium-influence never approximated the authority of a fascination over me, I willingly and finally abjured it as an impossible relief.
It was some time after this that my constitution, broken down by hard work, which, corporeally, to use an intensely idiomatic term, was much more “cruel on me” than hashish had been at its most nerve-racking stages, demanded not only rest, but something immediately tonic. The former was easily attained by closing my connection with the educational “Knight of the Rueful Countenance”﻿—a connection which all the while had not been chemical, like that of an acid with a base, but mechanical, like that of a force with a lever. The latter (the tonic) was to be found ultimately in exercise; but, for the sake of more instantaneous relief from debility, at the advice of a physician, I had recourse to spirits. A very short trial of their effect having convinced me that their stimulus was as dangerous as opium, I abandoned this also as a means of relief. The experiment made with it renewed, sometimes for two days together, the clarity, though not the exquisite beauty of the hashish visionary state, and repeated, in due succession, its ideal sufferings of night and daylight.
Thus taught that every possible stimulus of any power must invariably act as auxiliary to the partially routed forces of my foe, I called in no more treacherous helps from without, but went single-handed to the fight, armed only with patience and friendly sympathies.
Since learning this lesson, the progress into recovery has been by slow degrees, yet a progress after all.

===Chinese===

其效果无一例外极其恶劣，它并未像重拾大麻嗜好那样将我超拔入昔日充满愉悦活力与对周遭事物抱有兴致的层面，反倒诡异地同我体内可能残留的任何大麻残余力量交织纠缠，再次为我披上了那袭令昔日旧生活最黑暗片段变得痛彻心扉的惨淡裹尸布。疯癫的面孔对我横眉怒目；可怖的预言之声即便在我完全清醒时亦向我低语呢喃；我心中充盈着对某场迫在眉睫的天谴降临的凶险预感，并在痛定思痛中悲哀地发觉：自己不过是用一盏苦杯换取了另一盏苦杯罢了。鉴于鸦片的影响从未在我身上产生过任何接近诱惑魔力的权威，我心甘情愿且断然将其作为一种行不通的救济手段彻底舍弃。

正是在此后不久，我因严酷的繁重工作而彻底垮掉的体质——用一句极具表现力的口语来说，在肉体层面上这比大麻在最摧残神经的阶段对我更为“残酷无情”——不仅苛求休养生息，更迫切需要某种即刻的提振滋补。前者通过斩断我同那位教育界“愁容骑士（Knight of the Rueful Countenance）”的干系而轻易达成——那段关系自始至终皆非化学性的，宛如酸与碱的交融；而是机械性的，恰似外力与杠杆的生硬拼凑。而后者（提振滋补）最终当从身体锻炼中去寻觅；然而为了更迅速地从虚弱无力中解脱，在一位医生的建议下，我诉诸了烈酒。对烈酒效力极其短暂的试用便使我确信：其刺激性同鸦片一样凶险致命，我随即亦将此作为解脱手段彻底放弃。借由烈酒所进行的实验，有时会一连两天重新唤醒大麻异象状态的澄澈清晰（尽管毫无其精致绝伦之美），并按部就班地复现其在黑夜与白昼中的意念痛苦折磨。

由此我领教到了这深刻的一课：世间凡具任何强效的可能刺激物，皆必将充当已被我部分击溃的死敌的得力仆从，我不再从外部召集任何背信弃义的援兵，而是赤手空拳独自投入战斗，唯以耐心与朋友的深情关爱为铠甲。

自从汲取了这一教训之后，重返康复的步伐虽步履维艰，但归根结底却是在切实向前迈进。

===Original===

Ever and anon a return of the former suffering has made it necessary to spend half the night in walking; but the sense that every step forward was also a step, however infinitesimal, upward, is a greater relief than the possibility of once more journeying through the rosiest realms of the former hashish happiness. At least for the present﻿—as a proviso to the proposition let this be added﻿—for he who has once looked upon great glories can not but hope to behold them again, when nature is freed from all the grossness which makes them painful in the present state, and they shall come to him, not through walls which they must melt to make a passageway, but like the sunlight, which, falling joyously and harmlessly, bathes the forehead of the little child asleep.

===Chinese===

昔日痛苦的死灰复燃依然不时迫使我耗费半夜的时光来回踱步；然而，意识到前行的每一步同时亦是向上攀登的一步（无论多么微小），本身就是一种远超再度漫步于昔日大麻幸福最瑰丽王国的莫大解脱。至少在当下而言——作为这一论断的附带前提且容我添上一句——因为凡曾目睹过伟大荣耀之人，绝无法不期盼有朝一日能再度凝视它们：届时大自然将彻底摆脱在当下状态中令其痛苦不堪的一切粗鄙凡胎，而那些荣耀亦将向他迎面扑来，不再穿透它们必须熔化方能开辟通道的厚壁，而是宛若欢欣且无害倾泻的明媚阳光，温柔抚慰着酣睡孩童的纯洁前额。

## Notes on the Way Upward / 攀升途中的札记
===Original===

It is the author of “The Golden Dagon,” one of our most original and interesting American books of travel, who gives to Buddha, as the deity of eternal absorption, the most appropriate title with which he has ever, to my knowledge, been glorified. He calls him “The Stagnant Calm.” As I read it, such peculiar relevancy did this title seem to hold to one part of my own experience, that, but for occasional twinges of remaining humanity, remembered as having afflicted me about that time, I should have yielded to the conviction that I had myself then been an incarnation of Buddha. Hitherto my narrative has been of spell and counter-spell; of ecstasies bought on this side of Acheron, where the market was low, and paid for on the other side, where the rate of exchange is diabolic; of the checkered days of indulgence, and the one starless night of abandonment. It was during this latter period that the Buddhist state occurred. For many a month before I had been bathed in the springs of a fiery activity. I had lived in ether. Every sense had been worked at its highest power, the sense of the body, and the unspeakably more energetic sense of the imagination.
Now the exalting agency was removed. I have said how I suffered, affirmatively, from its lack in preternatural nightmares, in disgust at what seemed to me the lifeless forms of the outer world, in countless modes of pain and weariness, whose detail would be only less disagreeable to my reader than originally grievous to me. Far be it from me to recount these things again; indeed, for the past I have sometimes feared that I owed an apology, and might be expected to say, with him who had reduced courtliness to a science, “Pardon me, gentlemen, that I am so long in dying.”
But, negatively, as the months of trial went on, I came into a state which, had it been pain, would have made me fear less for myself. Gradually, after having for a long time known what it was to say, “Now I am perfectly wretched,” occurred seasons whose intervals constantly lessened when I said, “Now I am totally null.” It was not happiness any more than the rolling of a ball is sustained motion; like it, I went on mechanically by the not utterly extinct momentum of a removed force.
This force, too, was an hourly retarded one.

===Chinese===

正是《金达贡》（*The Golden Dagon*）——我们美利坚最富原创性与趣味性的游记著作之一——的作者，将佛陀视作永恒沉浸之神明，并赋予了他据我所知世间曾用以称颂他的最恰如其分的头衔。他称佛陀为“停滞之寂（The Stagnant Calm）”。当我读到这个词时，这头衔同我自己经历的某一部分之间显得具有如此非同寻常的契合关联，以至于若非清楚记得在彼时彼刻尚有一息残存的人性时不时以阵痛折磨着我，我恐怕早已屈从于自己当时正是佛陀真身化身的笃信念头了。迄今为止，我的叙述始终关乎魔咒与反魔咒；关乎在阿刻戎河（Acheron）此岸以低廉的市场价格购得、却在彼岸以恶魔般的汇率偿付代价的狂喜；关乎纵欲沉沦的斑驳白昼，以及戒断之后那片毫无星光的漫漫长夜。正是在这后一时期，降临了这般佛教徒式的虚无状态。在此前的漫长数月里，我一直浸泡在烈火般活跃生机的泉水之中。我曾栖息在以太纯阳之境。周身的每一处感官皆在以其最高功率运转——肉体的感官，以及想象力那无可言喻地更为充沛澎湃的超常感官。

如今，这股将人超拔升腾的媒介已被彻底抽离。我曾叙述过自己因失去它而在积极层面上遭受的苦楚——在超自然的噩梦中、在对我看来毫无生气的外部世界形态所油然而生的作呕反感中、在数不胜数的痛苦与倦怠模式中，若向读者详述这些细节，其令人不快的程度恐怕仅次于最初落在我身上时的深重创痛。我绝无意愿去重温复述这些陈年旧事；事实上，对于过往的篇幅我有时甚至担忧自己欠读者一声歉意，或许我当效仿那位将宫廷礼仪化作一门科学的君王，道一声：“诸位先生，原谅我死得这般慢腾腾的。”

然而，在消极层面上，随着考验岁月的推移，我步入了一种倘若果真只是肉体剧痛反倒能让我少为自身担忧几分的状态。在长久深知痛呼“如今我彻底痛苦不堪”的滋味之后，渐渐地降临了间隔越来越短的一连串时刻，每当彼时我只能叹道：“如今我彻底归于虚无。”那绝非幸福，正如一枚滚动的弹丸向前滚落绝非持续的主动运动一样；宛若弹丸一般，我仅仅依靠已被抽离的外力尚未彻底熄灭的惯性冲量，在机械麻木中被动向前滑行。

而这股惯性力，亦在以每小时为尺度不断衰减迟滞。

===Original===

There was constantly less and less hope, less volition, less interest, and the only offset to this negation was the opposite negation of disagreeable emotions. I did not despair, because there seemed nothing to despair of.
What should I do? Often (for this state of nonentity was only occasional as yet) I was visited by stern self-reprovings, admonitions to bestir myself spiritually as well as mechanically, threatenings of a final absorption into utter listlessness unless I resorted to some immediate means for quickening the pulses of thought and action.
Good people told me to sleep; Nature was reading me a lesson upon the curative properties of quiet. Good people, I could not sleep. I should never wake up again. Moreover, I attended another church of Nature’s, where the lesson for the day was, “He that will not work, neither let him eat;” and the margin was illuminated, not with cherubs like Raphael’s, who have nothing to do except to rest their chins upon their palms, but with certain others, sitting in rows upon a bench, diversifying their hopeless stare at the topmost pippins of the tree of knowledge by the furtive conveyance, from pocket to pocket, of a baser variety of apple, smuggled into school for the stay and consolation of the outer man which perisheth with the using.
This being the exact state of things until I left behind me, with my fulfilled responsibilities, that portentous and uncomfortable ghost, in whom my previous relations had forced me to behold Duty most eccentrically making herself incarnate, there were strong reasons for activity, besides its necessity as an energy of existence. In dissolving my connection with the portent, the latter reason still remained, and the question was how to satisfy it.
There was no further possibility of seeking activity in a research through supernatural passages. Stimulus had been abjured; the accumulation of mental facts, to serve as food for wonder, under its influence, was finished. Reason, Right, Well, all asserted this. There remained for me but one expedient.
This was to take the facts already secured, and discover, if possible, their meaning, their relations to each other; to crystallize them around the axis of some hypothesis, and determine what they taught of the operations of their source, the mind.

===Chinese===

希望日益渺茫，意志日益消沉，兴致日益衰减，而对这种全盘否定的唯一对冲补偿，乃是对令人不快之情绪的相反抵消。我并未感到绝望，因为周遭似乎已无任何值得绝望之事。

我究竟该如何是好？常常（因为这种虚无缥缈的虚无状态迄今尚属偶发），严厉的自我谴责便会造访心头，严词告诫我必须在属灵层面以及机械层面上振作起来，警示我除非立即采取某种立竿见影的手段去唤醒思想与行动的脉搏，否则终将彻底消融沉沦于绝对的萎靡不振之中。

善心的人们劝我安歇就寝；大自然正在向我布道静养疗愈的特质。善良的人们啊，我根本无法入眠。我深恐自己一旦阖眼便永不再能苏醒。更有甚者，我造访了大自然的另一座教堂，那里当天的每日经训乃是：“若有人不肯做工，就不可吃饭；”其书页边缘所配的泥金插画，并非拉斐尔笔下那些除了托腮呆坐之外无所事事的小天使，而是排排坐在长凳之上的另外一些凡夫俗子——他们目光呆滞绝望地凝视着智慧之树最高枝头上的优质苹果，并借由在口袋与口袋之间鬼鬼祟祟地暗中传递某种更为劣质的苹果来打破这番呆滞——那是偷运进学堂里、用来支撑抚慰那随着使用而注定朽坏的肉体外壳的零嘴。

直到我交卸了圆满履行的职责、将那个迫使我从过往关系中目睹“义务”以最古怪方式化作其真身的凶险可怖幽灵彻底抛在身后之前，这确凿便是彼时彼刻事态的真实写照；除了作为维系生存活力的必然必需之外，世间尚有强有力的理由促使我必须保持积极的行动。在解除了我同那个不祥之物的瓜葛之后，后一重理由依然存在，问题在于该如何满足它。

借由超自然的幽径去追寻思想的活力已再无任何可能。刺激物已被我断然戒绝；在其影响下搜集累积精神事实以充当惊叹食粮的岁月已然告终。理性、正义与良知皆异口同声证实了这一点。留给我的唯有一条权宜良策。

那便是将业已斩获的客观事实悉数搜集，尽可能探寻出它们的真谛、它们相互之间的亲缘关联；将它们围绕在某项假说的轴心周围予以凝结晶化，并测定判定它们对于其源头泉眼——心智——的运作规律究竟提供了何种启示。

===Original===

It was in this way that I kept up the vital heat of thought for months, and battled against an all-benumbing lethargy. The results of this practice I now go on to give, without any pretension to group them into a system; not only lack of time, but of a sufficiently broad basis of experiment having prevented that. If I shall seem to have fixed the comparative positions of even a few outposts of a strange and rarely-visited realm, I shall think myself happy. To travel farther into the interior, even for the sake of science, would have required a heroism wearing the guise, as looked at in different directions, of the martyr or the suicide. Of the first of these titles I did not hold myself worthy, nor of the last desirous.
How far hashish throws light upon the most interior of the mental arcana is a question which will be dogmatically decided in two diametrically opposite ways. The man who believes in nothing which does not, in some way, become tangent to his bodily organs will instinctively withdraw himself into the fortress of what he supposes to be antique common sense, and cry “madman!” from within. He will reject all of experience under stimulus, and the facts which it has professedly evolved as truth, with the final and unanswerable verdict of insanity.
There is another class of men which has its type in him who, while acknowledging the corporeal senses as very important in the present nutriment and muniment of our being, is convinced that they give him appearances alone; not things as they are in their essence and their law, classified harmoniously with reference to their source, but only as they affect him through the different adits of the body. This man will be prone to believe that Mind, in its prerogative of the only self-conscious being in the universe, has the right and the capacity to turn inward to itself for an answer to the puzzling enigmas of the world. Mind, infinite Mind, to be sure, created them and must have known their law; as an inference, Mind, though finite, may still interrogate its own phenomena for the reasons of outer existences which, however grand, are far less majestic than itself, and may obtain a clue proportionally perfect.

===Chinese===

正是凭借这种方式，我将思想的生命温热维系了长达数月之久，同那场令万物皆麻木不仁的致命昏睡展开了顽强抗争。对于这场实践所斩获的心得成果，我如今在此据实道来，绝无将其拼凑成一套体系的狂妄自负；不仅因为缺乏充裕的时间，更因为缺乏足够宽广的实验基石，阻止了我迈出这一步。倘若我能自诩哪怕仅仅锚定了这片奇异且罕有人至的疆域之中的数座前哨据点的相对方位，我便当深感心满意足。若要进一步向腹地纵深挺进，即便为了科学的圣名，亦需要一种在不同视角审视下或披戴着殉道者外衣、或显露着自杀者面目的英雄主义气魄。对于前一项头衔我不敢自命配得，而对于后一项头衔我亦毫无向往之心。

大麻究竟在多大程度上能够照亮心智最幽深的隐秘奥秘，乃是一个将被以两种截然相反的方式断然武断裁决的课题。凡对不能以某种方式同其肉体器官相切触之物一概拒不采信之人，皆会本能地退缩缩入他自以为属于古老常识的堡垒之中，并从城垛内部向外高呼“疯子！”。他将凭借一句不可反驳的疯癫终审判决，断然否定在刺激物影响下的一切经历，以及据称作为真理被由此推演出的全部客观事实。

世间尚有另一类人，其典型代表乃是这样一种人：他在承认肉体感官于我们当下存在的滋养庇护具有举足轻重作用的同时，亦坚信感官所赋予他的不过是虚妄的表象罢了；绝非事物在其本质与法则之中的本相——与其源头相呼应而和谐归类分类的本相——而仅仅是当事物穿透肉体的不同孔道时对他产生冲击的投影罢了。此人将极易倾向于坚信：心智，凭借其作为宇宙间唯一的自我意识存在的崇高特权，拥有转向自身探求破解尘世重重谜团之答案的权利与能力。心智——无限的心智，确凿创造了它们且必定通晓它们的运行规律；由此推论，有限的心智，尽管受制于凡胎，亦依然能向自身的内在现象发起追问，探求那些即便再宏伟壮丽、其威严亦远逊于心智本身的外部存在之缘由动因，并由此获得成比例完美契合的指引线索。

===Original===

Arguing thus, the man, albeit a visionary, will recognize the possibility of discovering from mind, in some of its extraordinarily awakened states, a truth, or a collection of truths, which do not become manifest in his everyday condition. From this man, a few such pages as these may hope for a candid reading, if not for total assent.
Nor am I anxious to repel the charge of insanity which may be brought against the facts evolved by a hashish delirium. Indeed, the exaltation, in this narrative, has been repeatedly called an insanity. I only wish to be understood as believing that into some subjects the insane man can look farther than the sane. Let not idiocy here be confounded with insanity. The former is the extinction of all faculties; the latter, the extraordinary development of one faculty or a group of faculties, while the others lie comparatively dormant.
In the same way, therefore, that the characteristics of the plant are sought, not in the microscopic filaments and tissues of the germ (although they truly exist there), but in the expanded individual of the species, we may, more legitimately and with much better hope of success, search out the law of a given mental organ in its unusually than its usually developed state.

===Chinese===

循着这番理路推演，此人，纵然被称为耽于空想的幻视者，亦能辨识出一种可能性：即从心智在某些超常觉醒的状态之中，发掘出某种或某组在日常状态下无法显露真容的崇高真理。从这样的人身上，如本书这般区区数页的文字，即便未能获得全盘赞同，亦大可期盼获得一番赤诚公允的审读研阅。

面对可能强加给由大麻谵妄所推演出的事实之上的疯癫指控，我亦无意急于辩驳洗刷。事实上，在本部叙述之中，那种亢奋出神的状态已屡次三番被直呼为癫狂发狂。我唯独期盼读者能够体谅我的一点信念：即对于某些特定课题而言，癫狂之徒往往能比心智健全之人洞察得更为深邃。切莫将白痴痴呆同疯癫癫狂混为一谈。前者乃是一切心智机能的彻底熄灭；而后者，则是某一机能或某组机能的非凡爆发演进，而其余机能则相对处于休眠沉睡之中。

因此，正如植物的独特特征绝非在种胚微观纤细的丝络与组织中探寻（尽管它们确实实实在在地潜藏于其中），而是在该物种充分舒展的成熟个体中去把握一样，我们亦能更为合情合理且抱持着大得多的成功希望，在某个特定心智器官异乎寻常展开的状态之中、而非在其寻常平庸的发育状态之中，去探寻昭示其运转的永恒法则。

## Labyrinths and Guiding Threads / 迷宫与指路明线
===Original===

Gentle reader﻿—not to make this one of my speculations more labyrinthine than nature, for I hate unnatural mysteries﻿—I will not, after the manner of an oracle, leave my title undefined until the sequel, but will here tell thee that the “labyrinths” are our bodily senses through which the outer world wanders in to commune with the soul. For a little while let us wander in together after the manner of the world, and if the clue of my speculation bring us not to the penetralia as surely as that of Ariadne, we may at least promise ourselves a safe-conduct out again. Let us try to discover the kind of communion which the world and the soul are holding together, and the manner in which they hold it.
Long before I had known hashish, and walked its weird uplands in pursuit of the secrets of mind, a revelation flashed upon me which, by its powers of amazement and perplexity, made the time and place of its occurrence forever memorial within me. It was a revelation in the same way that lightning is a revelation, clear in itself, yet showing hitherto unknown hills of unbroken midnight in the distance. While yet a mere boy, I was standing one afternoon by the side of two thinkers who talked metaphysics without taking me into their counsels, for they had no thought of my busying myself with anything but the outside of nature as I met her laughing in my rambles.
“Yes, it is beyond dispute that our senses give us only appearances and not things﻿—certain qualities of the essence, not the essence out of which they rise.”
In these words there was nothing to frighten a mind of ordinarily reflective habits; no barricade of “subjective” and “objective,” or anything else technical which I had not yet learned to scale. I was smitten with a sudden interest; I did not perfectly appreciate the meaning of the sentence, but wandered to a little distance to sit down and think it over till I had made it mine. There was a meaning there which held out the strongest fascinations to discovery.
“Our senses give us only appearances, qualities, and not things.” Perhaps, thought I, this is only a sophism hurled down as a sort of challenge for argument. These metaphysicians love to argue.
Of course, I did not have to look far for a test.

===Chinese===

温和的读者啊——为了不使我这篇思辨文章比大自然本身更为错综复杂（因为我平生最恨故弄玄虚的非自然奥秘）——我不会效仿神谕的做派将标题的深意悬置待续，而在此便径直相告：所谓的“迷宫”，正是我们的肉体感官，外部世界正是借由它们漫步深入、与灵魂交融神会的。且让我们一同依照世俗的方式漫步深入片刻；倘若我思辨的指引线索未能如阿里阿德涅之线（Ariadne）那般确凿无疑地将我们引至至圣所深处，我们至少亦能向自己许诺安然折返的通行保障。且让我们探求一番：尘世与灵魂之间究竟维系着何种交融神会，以及它们究竟是以何种方式维系这种神会的。

早在结识大麻、并在其诡谲的高地上漫步求索心智的隐秘之前，我脑海中便曾闪过一道顿悟——凭借其所带来的惊愕与困惑，它降临的时空在我心中化作了永恒的铭刻。那是一场宛若闪电般的顿悟，其自身耀眼夺目，却映照出远方绵延在永恒子夜中此前未知的重重峰峦。尚是一介垂髫少年之时，某个午后我伫立在两位思想家的身旁，他们高谈阔论着形而上学，却并未将我纳入他们的议事之列，因为他们绝未料想过除了漫游时所邂逅的大自然含笑的外表之外、我竟然还会对任何其他深邃之物产生兴致。

“是的，无可争辩的是：我们的感官赋予我们的仅仅是表象而非事物本身——仅仅是本质的某些属性，而非属性所依托生发的本质本身。”

在这番言辞之中，对于具备寻常反思习惯的心智而言本无任何可怖之处；毫无诸如“主观”与“客观”这般令人望而却步的壁垒，亦无任何我当时尚未学会攀越的专门术语障碍。我被一阵突如其来的强烈兴致所击中；我虽未能完全参透这句话的精髓深意，却漫步走到不远处坐下，反复将其咀嚼反刍，直至将其化为己有。在彼处蕴含着某种对探索求知具有至高诱惑力的深刻意蕴。

“我们的感官赋予我们的仅仅是表象、属性，而非事物本身。”我想，或许这不过是一则作为辩论挑战而随手抛出的诡辩吧。这群形而上学家向来酷爱争辩。

理所当然，我无须舍近求远去寻觅检验的试验场。

===Original===

I was leaning against a tree, and Sense, in the support given me by its trunk, seemed to be triumphantly asserting her acquaintance with things﻿—stanch and stout things at that.
But hold! I said to myself; what do I find out in leaning here, which makes me think that I have found a thing? Why, resistance, hardness, to be sure. And it is a fact, these are qualities only. But this is nothing but feeling; let me try the senses of smell and taste. By applying nose and tongue to the tree, I perceived a fresh woody savor﻿—quality still! I put my ear to the tree and struck it: still nothing but quality resulted, the capability to beget sound. I began to be alarmed for the dignity of the Sense, as I saw her chance of proving herself worthy of my past consideration narrowed down to one single organ﻿—the eye. Alas for her! Quality still﻿—a brown tint, a faculty of transmitting certain rays of light, and absorbing others. It seems strange now, but it is true that, with my knife, I began blazing the side of the tree, with a sort of fond flattery of the Sense that, though the qualities lay in the bark, “the thing” was to be detected lurking underneath. In a moment, however, I laughed perplexedly, realizing that I could make the matter no better if I hacked the tree through.
Here ended my first lesson upon the domain of the senses. I know that this incident in itself can claim no such interest as to make it part of an experience which one man, without obtrusiveness, may press upon another’s ear; but I have related it, believing that it may recall to some reader here and there the circumstances under which he made the same discovery. Still further, I mention it, since it may be a sort of common ground of sympathy between author and reader, upon which will be better understood something which I wish to say upon the philosophic sufferings of a great mind which it is our duty to appreciate as well as (and indeed in order to) pity.
David Hume, after having been fêted, buried, and reviewed, has been quietly laid upon the shelf by many serious men of the present century, in that especial niche devoted to “celebrated infidels.” According to our different acceptance of the term, this verdict will be just or unjust. If just, a careful and discriminating generation ought to manifest their coincidence with it by permitting him to lie under the index of obloquy.

===Chinese===

我正倚靠在一棵大树旁，而感官凭借其树干给予我的坚实支撑，似乎正在洋洋自得地宣称它对事物的熟稔——且是确凿结实的事物。

然而且慢！我自言自语道：我倚靠在此所发现的、促使我认为自己觅得了一件客观事物的凭据究竟为何？毫无疑问，乃是阻力与坚硬。而事实确凿，这些皆不过是属性而已。但这仅仅是触觉的范畴；且让我试探一下嗅觉与味觉。将鼻翼与舌尖贴在树干上，我感知到了一股清新的木质气味——依然是属性！我将耳朵贴在树干上并轻轻叩击：结果引发的依然不过是属性——产生声响的潜质。我开始为感官的尊严捏一把汗，因为眼见它向我证明自己配得上昔日尊崇的指望，已然被迫收缩退守至唯一的单一器官——眼睛。哀哉感官！依然是属性——一抹褐色的色泽，一种反射某些特定光线并吸收其他光线的机能。如今想来颇显古怪，但事实确凿的是：我掏出小刀，开始剥剔刮削树干的一侧，心中还带着一丝对感官的自欺安慰——满以为属性尽管附着在树皮之上，“事物本身”定然隐匿潜伏在皮层之下。然而仅过了片刻，我便困惑地苦笑出声，清醒意识到即便将整棵树砍倒劈透，我亦不可能让真相水落石出分毫。

至此，关于感官领地的第一堂课便告一段落。我深知这段轶事本身并无多么引人入胜的戏剧性，不足以构成一个人可以毫无冒犯之嫌硬塞入旁人耳畔的传奇经历；但我之所以将其娓娓道来，乃是深信这或许能在读者诸君中唤起某些人在同样契机下做出相同发现的往昔回忆。更有甚者，我之所以提及它，亦是为了在作者与读者之间铺设一种同理共鸣的立足基石——借此基石，将能更为深刻透彻地理解我接下来所要探讨的关于一颗伟大心灵所承受的哲思苦难——对其予以理解鉴赏（且委实是为了对其予以同情体恤），乃是我们的职责所在。

大卫·休谟（David Hume），在历经生前的受人追捧、身后的入土安葬与无数书评检视之后，已被本世纪的诸多严肃人士悄然搁置在书架之上，归入了专供“著名异端（celebrated infidels）”陈列的特定壁龛之中。依照我们对这一词汇的不同理解，这道判决或许公允，亦或许不公。倘若公允，一个谨严且具鉴别力的世代自当借由任凭他沉沦在千夫所指的耻辱柱下，来彰显与该判决的高度共识。

===Original===

If unjust, the sentence will, sooner or later, infallibly be reversed, and whatever light, however slight a pencil any man possesses for the illustration of the matter, is due no less to truth than to the shade of a philosopher.
Infidelity properly classifies itself under two divisions﻿—infidelity of the heart and infidelity of the intellect. The first of these is a malignant displeasure at truth for the obligations which it imposes upon life. It begins in a powerfully-felt repulsion between righteousness and the selfish will; it sometimes goes avowedly no farther, but leaves a man unjust, licentious, and in all respects, where the prudence of selfishness does not itself curb him, totally iniquitous.
In the case, however, of those who have carried on the offensive warfare of infidelity, one step farther has been taken, an utter and public rejection, namely, of the claims of truth upon self-interest. With this step has been conferred the degree, if I may so speak, of Grand Master of the Order of Heart Infidelity. It is not necessary that the man thus advanced should be preeminent, even above believers, in the prodigal gratification of passion and interest; temperament, society, a multiplicity of circumstances may serve as steering oars to his course, but circumstances only will direct him. The impelling force to any imaginable excess is present with him, and the certain compass of a felt obligation is gone. According to circumstances, he will go large before the wind with the graceful curvetings of a Bolingbroke, or stagger in a drunken sea like Paine.
The infidelity of the intellect is an entirely different thing. It arises, not from a hatred, but from an incorrect apprehension of truth.
When we remember how fundamental a part of human nature it is to systematize the dicta both of the written and the unwritten revelations, to build up the fragmentary formulas which express the manifold relations of our being into something like an orderly edifice, we must wonder, not so much that error infallibly vitiates to an extent more or less fatal some part of the workmanship, as that any structure so far resists gravity as not to tumble down.

===Chinese===

倘若不公，这道判决迟早必将被无可挽回地彻底推翻，而世人所拥有的哪怕最微弱的一缕能照亮此事的笔触光芒，无论对于真理本身，还是对于一位哲学家的亡魂而言，皆是不可推卸的应尽之责。

所谓“不信/异端（infidelity）”，按其性质可恰如其分地划归为两大门类——心灵的不信与智识的不信。前者乃是对真理所施加于生命的道德义务怀抱恶意的反感排斥。它肇始于公义正直与自私意志之间被深切感知到的强烈抵触冲突；有时在公开层面上它并未走得更远，却任由一个人沦为不公、放荡之徒，且在凡自私的精明算计未曾予以制约的一切方面，皆表现得彻头彻尾罪孽深重。

然而，在那些对信仰发起正面进攻作战之人的案例中，他们更进一步迈出了决定性的一步——即公然且彻底摒弃真理对利己之心的道德约束主张。伴随着这一步的迈出，此人便被授予了我姑且称之为“心灵不信骑士团大宗师”的显赫头衔。被擢升至此境之人，未见得必然在纵欲与谋利的挥霍放浪上超越信徒；其性情气质、所处社会、以及形形色色的错综复杂环境，皆充当着拨弄其航向的摇橹舵桨，但也唯有环境能左右他的前行。驱使他迈向一切可想象之极端的驱动冲力始终深植于他体内，而道德义务那确凿清晰的罗盘指南针却已荡然无存。视具体境遇而定，他或许会伴随着博林布鲁克（Bolingbroke）那般优雅自如的马步在顺风中扬帆破浪，亦或许会在醉醺醺的波涛中宛若潘恩（Paine）一样踉跄蹒跚。

智识的不信则是一桩截然不同之事。它绝非源于对真理的仇恨，而是源自对真理的失当理解与错误把握。

当我们忆及将成文与不成文天启神示的种种信条体系化、将描摹我们存在之繁复关系的零散公式构建成一座井然有序的严整大厦乃是人类本性何其根本的一部分之时，我们便不得不感到惊叹——并非惊叹于谬误不可避免地在某种或大或小具有致命性的程度上玷污了整个工巧的某一部分，而是在于世间竟然有任何建筑结构能够抵御万有引力的侵蚀而不至于彻底轰然倾塌。

===Original===

Not that this imperfection is to be ascribed to the habit of systematizing, but to the fact that it is human nature which systematizes﻿—human nature, which never in any one age sweeps all truth in a comprehensive view and realizes at once the tendencies of opinions, but of necessity looks at half truths through a distorting medium, and sees only the present result of speculations. A cornerstone laid awry, some premise whose falsity is unnoticed because it has the sanction of antique opinion, may render the whole superstructure out of line and unstable, although it be reared by the most cautious workmen with unsparing scrutiny of square and plummet. In a former century, while men were contented merely with the foundation walls of a system, it mattered little whether every block was laid with perfect accuracy; there was as yet no edifice to be affected in its permanency by the error of the groundwork. But when, in the course of time, “other men builded thereon,” accepting it with perfect faith as the careful structure of a master whose name was spoken reverently among men, what wonder that they pointed afterward to the marks of considerateness and caution with which they had built up their secondary walls of inference into a philosophy, as a proof that they must necessarily be stable and faultless, however much some of their compeers doubted it, though refusing to acknowledge any fault in the foundation?
The infidels of intellect have as often resulted from arguing logically upon some falsehood, hitherto universally accepted as a truism, as from any distortion of real truths of sophistical deductions from good grounds. That, if Hume was an infidel, he became one thus, we think it easy to show. Almost as easy is it to prove that, properly speaking, he was not an infidel at all.
As a central point for the consideration of Hume’s infidelity, let us take the year 1746, the year in which he stood candidate for the Edinburgh chair of Moral Philosophy, and by the vote of the authorities (no doubt with the most perfect propriety) was defeated on account of his views of religion. Against the action which excluded him from a professorship so rigorously demanding an incumbent of Spartan principles upon the subject which was to be his speciality, certainly no thinking man can have aught to say.

===Chinese===

这缺陷绝不可单纯归咎于热衷体系化的习癖，而应归因于推动体系化构建的主体乃是人类本性这一客观事实——人类本性在任何单一时代皆从未能以俯瞰全景的宏阔视野包揽全部真理，亦绝无法即刻洞悉诸多观念的深远走向，而是必然透过折射失真的扭曲介质来管窥半截真理，且仅能看清思辨当下所结出的即时硕果。一块砌偏了的基石，某个因拥有古老观念背书而未被察觉其荒谬的前提假设，便足以令整座上层建筑失去准星、摇摇欲坠，纵使它是由最谨慎严谨的工匠运用水准尺与铅垂线进行不遗余力的严苛审视所精心筑就。在前一个世纪里，当人们尚且满足于某套体系的地基墙体之时，每一块石料是否皆以绝对的精度严密垒砌原本无伤大雅；当时尚无整座巍峨宏伟的殿宇会因地基的偏差而危及其长存根基。然而随着岁月的流逝，当“后人在此基础之上添砖加瓦”，怀着至诚的笃信将其接纳为一位在世人中间备受尊崇的宗师的严谨杰作之时，他们在日后将自身推演出的哲学派生副墙所展现出的周全深思与审慎考量视作证明其必然稳固无瑕的铁证，这又何足为奇呢？——尽管其某些同侪对此深表怀疑，却又无一例外拒绝承认地基本身存在着任何纰漏。

智识层面的异端，往往正如从良好根基中推导出诡辩结论或歪曲真实真理一样，频频源自对某项此前被普世奉为公理的不移假象展开严丝合缝的逻辑推演。倘若休谟果真称得上一介异端，他正是通过这种途径沦为异端的，对此我们认为极易加以阐明。而且几乎同样容易证明的是：严格说来，他根本算不上一介真正的异端。

作为审视休谟异端倾向的核心原点，且让我们将目光聚焦于1746年——在这一年，他是爱丁堡大学道德哲学教席的候选人，最终却因其宗教观念在校方权威的投票表决中（毫无疑问，这完全合乎体统规矩）惨遭败北。对于将他排除在一处如此严苛苛求履职者在其专业领域必须抱持斯巴达般严谨原则的教授席位之外的决断，凡有识之士显然皆无任何微词可言。

===Original===

The fact of the exclusion is mentioned merely for the sake of determining some date when his bias was generally recognized among the people, who had treated with such neglect his “Treatise on Human Nature,” published nine years before. In 1746, then, he had reaped the title of infidel.
For at least half a century previous, the speculative mind of the greater part of Europe (dynamically as well as numerically greater) had been under the dominion of John Locke, whose “Essay upon the Human Understanding” had been brought to light in 1690. It is perhaps rather an insincere compliment to speak of any mind as “speculative” which expatiated merely within his prescribed area. The system which bore his name is too well known to ask a statement, especially within these limits. Its parent he could hardly be called; certainly not with any more justice than we could ascribe to the man who casually remarks that it is a cloudy day the parentage of that meteorological phenomenon. His system consists mainly in the discovery that people generally get such and such ideas about their thinking faculty; that the said people have pretty nearly hit the nail on the head, and that he is glad to tell them so; all authenticated by John Locke, his mark. The majority of mankind attend to the knowledge secured through their bodily organs more closely than to any other; they elaborate truth by thinking upon this knowledge; and thus all truth comes to us through the organs, modified to a greater or less extent by reflection. In fine, sense, and reflection on its data, the sources of all knowledge, form the governing principle, the “*articulum stantis aut cadentis ecclesiae*” of the Lockian philosophy.
Into this philosophy Hume, like all the other contemporary minds of his nation, was born as regularly as into the monarchical form of government. It was the nursery of his childhood and the school of his youth; his mind, when it wanted exercise, must run out and play in John Locke’s small back yard, or not stretch its limbs at all.
Now there came a time when David Hume arrived at the very same point of speculation which I have previously mentioned as reached, on my supposition, by most of us who think. Let us see how he reasoned. Suppose him in soliloquy:
“I find that my senses give me nothing but the phenomena of things﻿—tell me merely how objects act upon me.

===Chinese===

提及这次被拒的事实，纯粹是为了确定一个标志着他的观念偏向已然在公众中间被普遍辨识的时间节点——而公众在九年前对他出版的《人性论》（*Treatise on Human Nature*）曾予以了何等冷落漠视。那么，在1746年，他便已然坐实了“异端”的头衔。

在此前至少半个世纪的时光里，欧洲绝大部分地区（无论在动态声势上还是在人数规模上皆占绝对优势）的思辨心智，皆一直笼罩在约翰·洛克（John Locke）的统治之下——其《人类理解论》（*Essay upon the Human Understanding*）于1690年问世。将任何仅仅在他所划定的既定疆域内徘徊驰骋的心智称为“思辨性”的，或许算不上一句真诚由衷的恭维。冠以他大名的这套体系世人皆太为熟稔，无须在此详加赘述，尤其是在眼下的篇幅之内。他很难算得上是这套体系的生身之父；正如我们绝无法将某位随口提及今日天色阴沉的人视作该气象现象的生身始祖一样。他的体系主要在于发掘出人们通常对其思维机能抱有这般那般的观念；在于指出上述世人差不多正好切中了要害，而他乐于向世人证实这一点；所有这一切皆加盖了约翰·洛克的专属印信。绝大多数凡人专注于通过自身肉体器官所斩获的知识，其热切程度远超对任何其他维度的关注；他们借由对这些知识的思辨推演来淬炼真理；因此所有的真理皆穿透器官向我们涌来，并在或多或少的程度上经受反思的修饰转化。总而言之，感官以及对其数据的反思作为一切知识的源泉，构成了洛克哲学的统治原则，构成了其“教会立或倒的枢纽关节点（*articulum stantis aut cadentis ecclesiae*）”。

正如同胞生来便自然而然置身于君主制政体之下一样，休谟亦理所当然地降生在了这套哲学体系之中。那是他童年的摇篮与青年的学堂；他的心智每逢渴求锻炼之时，要么跑到约翰·洛克那逼仄狭小的后院里嬉戏玩耍，要么便唯有彻底蜷缩四肢一动不动。

然而终有一日，大卫·休谟抵达了我先前假定我们绝大多数善于思考之人皆曾涉足的完全相同的思辨节点。且让我们审视他是如何推理论证的。让我们设想他内心的独白：

“我发现我的感官赋予我的无非是事物的现象表象——仅仅告知我客观物体究竟是如何作用于我的。”

===Original===

My eye acquaints me with color and outline; my ear with vibrations of diverse intensities; and so on with all the rest of the organs. All give qualities of things, operations which things have a capability to perform on me, appearances of things, but never *things* themselves. How do I know that they do not? By reflection, certainly; reflection on the data afforded by sense. But why do we all believe, and act upon the belief, that we see, hear, feel, smell, and taste *things*? It must no doubt be sense that tells us so; that is the only conjoint source of knowledge with reflection. Then I have within me, and so has everyone else, two exactly opposite verdicts. I *do* know *things*, and I *do not* know them. Now which is the lie?”
Hume did not decide. He did not pretend to stand arbiter between these two conflicting juries, which Locke fifty years before had impaneled to settle infallibly, and without appeal, all the questions of human science. He only hung in perfect equipoise between the reality and the nonentity of all being, himself necessarily included. He became, as a strictly logical consequence of that teaching which he had drunk with his mother’s milk, and which he would have rejected as much in peril of being called an unnatural son by all his contemporaries, a Pyrrhonist, a universal doubter. And who, in the name of all candor, was the parent of his Pyrrhonism? Who but John Locke, who, while a believer himself, because he did not bowl far enough in his own direction, had nevertheless opened up an easy track to the most comprehensive system of skepticism in the universe.
There may be those who will think that we have made out no better case for Hume by proving him a skeptic than an infidel. What difference exists, they ask, between doubting and disbelieving? Every possible difference. Belief and unbelief are often wrongly taken as antipodes merely on account of their antagonistic sound, and doubting is often confounded with the latter. Unbelief is, in fact, the same mental act as belief, directed by evidence or passion to a different set of statements. Doubt recognizes an equiponderance of evidence, or a total lack of evidence on both sides. Now the impulses of hate, pride, and a thousand others may bear a man’s belief one way or another, and so vitiate the sincerity of a judgment which ought to found itself calmly on proof.

===Chinese===

“我的眼睛让我知晓色彩与轮廓；我的耳朵让我知晓不同强度的振动；其余所有感官器官亦复如是。它们赋予的一切皆是事物的属性，是事物有能力施加于我之上的作用，是事物的表象，却绝非事物（things）本身。我又是如何知晓它们并非事物本身的？当然是通过反思；对感官所提供的数据进行反思。然而，为何我们所有人都笃信、且依据这一信念行事——我们能看、听、感触、嗅闻并品尝到事物？毫无疑问，定然是感官如此告知我们的；因为感官是与反思并存的唯二知识来源。如此一来，我内心深处——亦如其余每个人一样——便并存着两份截然相反的裁决：我确实知晓事物，我又绝不知晓它们。那么，究竟哪一个是谎言？”

休谟并未作出决断。他并未妄图在这两组相互冲突的陪审团之间充当仲裁者——洛克在五十年前曾遴选召集这两组陪审团，试图让它们确凿无疑、不可上诉地裁决人类科学的一切疑问。他只是悬置于一切存在的实在性与虚无性之间那座完美的均势天平之上，必然亦将自身囊括在内。作为他自幼随母乳一同吮吸、若予摒弃便会在所有同侪眼中冒被视作不孝之子之险的那套学说的严密逻辑必然结果，他沦为了一介皮浪主义者（Pyrrhonist），一介普遍怀疑者。凭着一切坦荡赤诚扪心自问，究竟谁才是他这套皮浪主义的始作俑者？除了约翰·洛克还能有谁？洛克本人虽为一名信徒，仅因在自己的行进路线上未曾走得足够深远，却已然为全宇宙最包罗万象的怀疑主义体系铺就了一条平坦大道。

或许会有人认为，我们即便证明休谟是一介怀疑论者而非异端分子，亦并未为他洗清多少嫌疑。他们会发问：怀疑与不信之间究竟有何区别？存在着一切可能的区别。信念与不信往往仅仅因其对立的字面声响便被错误地视作两极对立，而怀疑又频频与后者混为一谈。事实上，不信乃是与信念完全相同的心智活动，不过是在证据或激情的驱使下指向了另一组不同的陈述罢了。怀疑所认定的，乃是双方证据的分量持平，或是两造皆彻底匮乏证据。然而，仇恨、傲慢以及成千上万的其他冲动，皆可能将一个人的信念推向某一方，从而破坏本应平心静气立足于证据之上的判断的赤诚公允。

===Original===

Doubt, where it is real, can never be thus produced by impulse. To sit upon the exact centre of the beam, it must be calm. Therefore, so far as any man is a sincere skeptic, so far is he proved guiltless of the charge of hostility to either side.
I do not assert this perfect calm for Hume. In the present imperfect condition of humanity we act so universally from intricately mixed motives, that it would not be safe to assert a purely ideal sincerity for anyone. Doubtless Hume was influenced in the after maintenance of his Pyrrhonist principles by many of those partisan considerations which weigh with us all. But in the first susception of his doubt, acting, as he did, upon the everywhere acknowledged basis that Locke was right, no man could have been more logical, more calmly, philosophically sincere. Ratiocination, and not hostility to religion, was the original cause of his skepticism.
It is particularly unfortunate for a man when he is thrown into the society of those who, by flattering that in him which his better nature feels to be a blemish and a disadvantage, if not a crime, lull his pain at its existence, and even persuade him to believe that it is his honor. We have to observe an exemplification of such misfortune in Hume, who, but for being lauded and fêted as the Coryphaeus of infidels, for whom he felt no cordial attraction, might have outlived his skepticism through draughts of a better philosophy, or, at least, have kept it to himself as his most mournful secret.
Allowing himself to be applauded as the infidel which he was not, he fortified within himself the skeptic which he was; but that he never made a wholehearted consecration of himself, as some would misrepresent him, to the cause of a malignant and offensive unbelief, is evident from many facts in its history; such, for instance was his indignant rebuff of the pert wife of the atheist Mallet, who took the liberty of introducing herself to him at a soirée: “We freethinkers ought to know each other, Mr. Hume.” “I am no freethinker, madam;” and, turning on his heel, he strode angrily away.
There is a letter of his, also, which I only quote from memory, in which he exhibits the man he would have been if left alone, declaring that he never sat down to a game of chess with a friend, and thus threw off his logical panoply, without feeling his doubts vanish and the reality of things return.

===Chinese===

真正的怀疑，绝不可能由冲动所催生。欲稳坐于天平衡梁的正中央，心境必须平如止水。因此，一个人只要称得上一介真诚的怀疑论者，便足以证明他在对任何一方怀抱敌意的指控上皆是清白无辜的。

我并非断言休谟已达致这种全然的平静。在人类当下尚未完善的境况之中，我们的行事往往出于错综复杂的混合动机，因而断言任何人具备纯粹理想化的赤诚皆非审慎之举。毫无疑问，休谟在日后维系其皮浪主义原则的过程中，亦受到了许多对我们所有人皆产生分量的派系考量的左右。然而在他最初萌生怀疑的时刻，立足于世人普遍公认“洛克正确无误”这一基石之上，绝无任何人能比他更为逻辑缜密，更为平静、哲学式地真诚坦荡。纯粹的理性推演，而非对宗教的敌意，才是其怀疑主义的原初根由。

当一个人不幸置身于这样一群人之列时，委实是一桩莫大的悲剧：这些人极力奉承逢迎他本性中视为瑕疵与缺陷、甚或罪愆的特质，抚平了他因这些特质存在而承受的痛苦，甚至诱使他坚信这乃是他的无上荣光。我们在休谟身上便不得不目睹这样一出不幸的例证——若非因被奉为异端分子的领袖魁首（Coryphaeus）而饱受吹捧宴飨（而他对这些人实则毫无由衷的好感），他本可通过汲取更上乘的哲学养分而超脱其怀疑主义，或者至少能将其作为自身最哀戚的秘密深藏于心。

任由自己被当成他本非其人的异端分子而承受喝彩，他在内心深处进一步固化了自己实实在在所是的怀疑论者；但他绝未如某些恶意歪曲者所言，将自己全心全意奉献给恶毒且冒犯的不信事业，这一点从其生平历史的诸多事实中便昭然若揭；譬如，他曾对无神论者马利特（Mallet）那位在晚宴上冒昧向他自我介绍的冒失妻子报以愤怒的断然斥责：“休谟先生，我们自由思想家应当彼此相识。”“夫人，我绝非自由思想家；”他随即旋踵转身，怒气冲冲地阔步离去。

此外，亦有一封他的书信（我仅凭记忆引述），在信中他展现了若任其独处他本会成为的真实模样；他坦言自己每次与友人坐下对弈一局国际象棋、从而卸下身上的逻辑甲胄之时，无一例外皆会感到自己的种种怀疑烟消云散，万物的实在之感重返心头。

===Original===

Yet this very letter has been quoted in evidence of his insincerity, because, it is said, he was forced to reason that he might support his doubts. But what if reasoning infallibly sustained them? Was he to trust in Hume playing chess or in Hume reasoning?
By his unnatural conjunction with infidels, he subjected himself to bear the obloquy of their praise. By their praise, an antagonist spirit of denunciation was excited in the society of believers. Denounced, he must reply, for the sake of his pride and his partisans. And thus, from the sincerely perplexed doubter, he came to be considered, and in a certain, though a far less degree to be, the sneering foe of Christianity.
I have dwelt thus long upon Hume and the circumstances which have tended to give him his present reputation, and to set upon the stamp of an odium in many respects unjust, because he is an example not less striking than painful of the evil which may be wrought for a man by some unnoticed error in his mental philosophy. How easily an error which is the germ of all things hurtful may escape the notice of men who accept without examining, can be seen from the fact that the good John Locke (for he was good) was never advised of the skeptical inference from his doctrine, but died as perfectly satisfied with it as he had lived.
Most gratefully do I remember that, at the time of my first discovery of the legitimate domain of the senses, I was not left, like many others in similar case, and Hume as the representative of them all, to retreat hopelessly into a negation of all knowledge.
It is the privilege and the glory of this day that its dominant philosophy is Transcendental. Much as this word, like its kindred visionary, is in the mouth of hawkers of theological small ware﻿—much as it has been applied, by a perversion, to all systems of error and nonsense﻿—much as it has been branded for a stigma upon the forehead of thinkers who would not travel in a go-cart, the idea which it represents has been the regeneration of speculative philosophy. The Transcendentalists are, indeed, climbers over, as their name signifies, yet not over sound reasoning nor the definite principles of truth, but over that ring-fence of knowledge brought in through mere physical passages, with which a tyrannous oligarchy of reasoners would circumscribe all our wanderings in search of facts and laws.

===Chinese===

然而，正是这封书信却被引作证明他虚伪不诚的证据，因为据说他不得不诉诸推理才能维系其怀疑。然而，倘若理性推演确凿无疑地支撑着那些怀疑，他又当如何？他是应当信任弈棋时的休谟，还是应当信任推理时的休谟？

由于他同异端分子之间那种违背天性的勾连，他不得不承受因他们的赞许而招致的千夫所指。因着他们的赞许，信徒群体中激起了一股针锋相对的声讨浪潮。身遭声讨，出于自尊与党同之谊，他又不得不奋起还击。于是，从一介真诚困惑的怀疑者，他逐渐被视作——且在某种程度上（尽管程度要轻得多）确实沦为了——基督教的冷嘲热讽之敌。

我之所以在休谟以及促成其当下声名并给他打上在诸多方面皆显不公的恶名烙印的种种情由上着墨如此之多，乃是因为他作为一个例证，极其鲜明且痛苦地昭示出：心智哲学中某个未被察觉的错谬，能给一个人带来何等巨大的荼毒。一项作为一切有害事物萌蘖的错谬，在不加审视便全盘接纳的人们眼前是何等容易溜过，从这一事实中便可见一斑：善良的约翰·洛克（因为他确实称得上善良）生前从未被告知其学说中所蕴含的怀疑论推论，而是终其一生对其深信不疑，直至安详辞世。

我满怀至深的感激铭记着：在我初次发掘出感官的合法疆界之时，我并未如处于类似境地的诸多其他人那样——以休谟作为他们全员的典型代表——无可奈何地绝望退守至对一切知识的彻底否定之中。

当今时代得天独厚的特权与荣光，正在于其统治性的哲学乃是先验主义（Transcendentalism）。尽管这个词如同其近亲“幻视者”一样，频频挂在神学小商贩的嘴边兜售；尽管它曾被曲解附会于一切错谬与荒唐的体系；尽管它曾作为烙印耻辱地打在那些不肯坐着学步车步履蹒跚的思想家的前额之上；然而它所代表的理念，却成就了思辨哲学的重生再造。正如其字面原义所示，先验主义者确实是一群“攀越者”——然而他们所攀越的，绝非健全的推理或确凿的真理法则，而是跨越了仅仅经由肉体孔道所传入知识的那道环形围栏；而那一小撮专横跋扈的推理者寡头，正企图用这道围栏禁锢圈定我们在探求事实与法则过程中的一切求索跋涉。

===Original===

Older than its oldest historic supporting names, Transcendentalism still found champions in the more enfranchised minds of Greece, and from them we come per saltum to its German champions of the latter half of the last, and the elapsed half of the present century. Kant, awakened, as there is some reason to think, by the very perplexity which set boundaries to the mind of Hume, stands forth as the resurrectionist of the long-buried idea, and is followed, with more or less nonessential departure from his main track, by Fichte, Hegel, and Schelling; for, although the first of the trio may be styled a pure idealist, he follows Kant preeminently in the assertion of far higher grounds of knowledge than the sense. It is complained that these men, and chief of all Kant, are unintelligible; that their phraseology is cumbrous and obscure. It is not difficult, however, for any mind in charity with the direction of their efforts to see abundant reason why it should be so.
In the first place, while their language (when they did not write in Latin, and when they did their German modes of thought still went with them) is the most plastic in the world to all the moulds of mind, while it admits of endless word-compounding to give roundness or definiteness to ideas, still, from this very fact, it tends toward obscurity, for the reason that the compounds so lengthen a sentence as to make it very difficult to carry the meaning from beginning to end.
In the second place, it is to be remembered that the ideas which these men had to communicate were to a great extent new﻿—new even to one who looked at them fragmentarily﻿—new particularly in their combinations as a system. They who set them forth were the pioneers of Transcendentalism; they had nothing ready to their hand, nothing open or clear; and the first entrance into a territory is always of necessity by a rugged path; it is for those who enter into their labors, who come in upon the ground which they have opened, to attend to grading the causeway. First the military road, after that the turnpike. As well may we quarrel with Captain John Smith for not laying a railway through the forests of Virginia, as with Kant for not smoothing the passage into a philosophy through which he was the first traveler.

===Chinese===

先验主义的历史比其在历史上最早有记载的支持者的名号更为久远，它在古希腊那些更为自由开明的心智中便已找到了捍卫者；从古希腊出发，我们跃进（per saltum）至上一世纪后半叶及本世纪前半叶的德国捍卫者之列。正如某些理由所揭示的那样，正是那道禁锢休谟心智的困惑唤醒了康德（Kant），使他挺身而出，成为这一深埋已久的理念的起死回生者；在其身后，紧随其后的是费希特（Fichte）、黑格尔（Hegel）与谢林（Schelling），他们在不同程度上偏离了他的主轴轨道，但皆属细枝末节；因为尽管这三人中的第一位可被称为纯粹唯心主义者，但他追随康德的最核心之处，便在于断言存在着远高于感官的知识根基。有人抱怨这些人——尤其是康德——晦涩难解，其用词笨拙冗赘。然而，对于任何同情理解其探索方向的心智而言，看清其何以必然如此的充沛缘由绝非难事。

其一，他们的语言（当他们未用拉丁文写作时；而即便用拉丁文写作，其德语式的思维模式亦如影随形）虽是世间最契合心灵一切铸模的极具塑性的语言，虽容许无休止的复合造词以赋予观念圆润或明晰的质感，但恰恰源于这一特性，它亦倾向于陷入晦涩，因为复合词极大拉长了句式结构，以至于极难自始至终贯通维系全句的意涵。

其二，必须谨记的是，这些人所要传达的观念在很大程度上皆属前所未见的新知——即便对于仅以零散视角审视它们的人而言亦属新知，尤其是当它们组合构建为一套体系时更显新颖。阐发这些观念的人乃是先验主义的开路先锋；他们手头毫无现成现用之物，毫无坦途或清朗视野；初次踏入一片未知领地，必然只能跋涉于崎岖坎坷的歧路之上；唯有留待那些承接其劳绩、步入其所开辟基业的后继之人，去着手修葺平整大道基石。先有军用便道，随后方有收费坦途。我们苛求康德未能将其作为首位行者所踏足的哲学通路铺平理顺，无异于责怪约翰·史密斯船长（Captain John Smith）当年未能直接贯通穿越弗吉尼亚森林的铁路一样荒诞不经。

===Original===

It was enough for him that he had grappled with great ideas and fixed them; let his successors attend to polishing their surface.
