Third, it has been put out of sight by the prevalence of a philosophy which calls itself that of common sense, but is much worthier of being named that of commonplace, that metaphysics is as true and distinct a science as chemistry, with its own peculiar and inalienable ideas, and in virtue of that prerogative demands, both as necessity and right, symbols to express its ideas which shall be its own exclusive property. Let the phrases of distinction, “objective” and “subjective,” be an example. “Objective” is everything which, in the processes of mind, is not myself, but extraneous to me; “subjective,” all that is myself and my own individual part of the operation. Now the sense philosophy could have no possible use for any such words as these, since it recognizes nothing but a paper distinction between a man and his objects for all purposes of perception, all his knowledge being gained through sense, and flowing into him as its passive receptacle. So sense philosophy sneers at such technical phraseology as pedantic. But, supposing it capable of requiring some symbols for such ideas, it would most likely adopt “outward” and “inward.” For speculations, or rather assertions, so little analytic and accurate as its own, these might do well enough; but how inert, how useless, how vague would they be where any subtle mental fact was to be definitely expressed. We wish to give the idea of the mind as examined by itself. Transcendentalists call this treating the mind “objectively.” Our sense men would be compelled to say, treating it “outwardly.” How definite would be the idea conveyed in that!
When we complain of the sailor for speaking of his masts as spars, instead of calling them sticks, to meet the comprehension of some landlubber who will not take the pains to learn practical navigation﻿—when the chemist is sneered at for saying crucible instead of pot﻿—when, in fine, public opinion shall compel all men to talk of the delicacies of their arts in street slang or boudoir twaddle, then, and not till then, will it be time to deride the science, wherein, more than all others, rigorous exactitude of expression is required, for having a peculiar, even though it be not a universally intelligible language.

===Chinese===

对他而言，能够紧紧擒获伟大的观念并将其牢牢确立，便已足矣；且由后继者去料理磨砺其表面光泽吧。

其三，由于一种自诩为“常识哲学”实则更配被称为“平庸哲学”的流派的盛行，人们忽视了一个事实：形而上学是一门同化学一样真实且独立的科学，拥有其特有且不可剥夺的观念；凭借这项特权，它无论出于必然需求还是正当权利，皆要求拥有专属其独家的符号来表达其观念。且以“客观的（objective）”与“主观的（subjective）”这一对辨析词汇为例：“客观的”是指在心智运转过程中非我的一切事物，是独立外在于我的事物；“主观的”则是指完全属于我自己以及属于我个人运作部分的一切。而感官哲学绝无可能派得上这些词汇的用场，因为在一切感知的目的上，它在人与其感知客体之间仅仅承认一种徒有其名的纸面界分，其一切知识皆经由感官获得，并作为被动接受的容器涌入其心智之中。因此，感官哲学将这类专门术语嗤之以鼻为迂腐学究气。然而，即便假定它需要某些符号来表达此类观念，它极有可能采纳“外部的”与“内部的”。对于像它自身那样毫无分析深度与精确度可言的思辨（或者说断言）而言，这些字眼或许差强人意；然而一旦需要清晰确切地表达某种精微幽深的心智事实，它们该是何等迟钝、无用与含混啊！我们希望表达“心智对自身展开审视”的观念；先验主义者称其为“客观地”对待心智；而我们的感官论者则不得不说成“外部地”对待心智。借由这种表述所传达的观念，又能具备何等明确的清晰度呢！

唯有当我们能够理直气壮地斥责水手将其桅杆称为“桁木”而非迁就某些不愿费心研习航海实务的旱鸭子的理解称为“木棍”之时——唯有当化学家因使用“坩埚”而非“瓦罐”而遭受讥讽被视作理所当然之时——简而言之，唯有当公众舆论强迫世人在谈及各自技艺的幽微精妙之处时一律改用市井俚语或闺阁闲话之时，方才到了去嘲弄一门比其余一切科学皆更严苛苛求表达绝对精确性的科学拥有专属其特有、即便并非人尽皆知的语言的时机。

===Original===

This talk about the pedantry of metaphysics is something which the age should be ashamed of as behind it, yet even now we occasionally light upon some reviewer who, in strains of touching pathos, laments to the public that he finds it impossible to read Hickok’s *Rational Psychology* to his wife of an evening on account of the doctor’s pedantic technicality, which makes him a sealed book even to that gifted woman.
In general, it is safe to lay down this proposition as a rule: first look cursorily over a book upon Mind, to see whether its general character for neatness and system proves that its author is neither fool nor sloven; and then, reading it through carefully and with candor, you will find that in proportion to its technicality is it the repository of new and deeper truths. This, of course, is to be understood of those books on Mind which, according to De Quincey’s division in his critique on Pope, belong to the literature of knowledge, and not the literature of power. The same habit of mental indolence, which is loosening the cords of our American literature﻿—the loving such books as read themselves to us while we lie half asleep on a sofa; the greed for dainties which may be swallowed whole, and which tickle at a moment’s warning﻿—this habit it is which has deprived of nine tenths of his legitimate number of readers such a man, for instance, as Hickok. Almost the only real metaphysician of America, perhaps the greatest now living anywhere, and worthy to be classed with the strongest and deepest thinkers of any age or land, he has, in his own country, about as many intelligent and appreciative readers as Pythagoras had of esoteric disciples. There is reason to fear that men love better to investigate how muslins, hay-rakes, and, above all and inclusive of all, money may be made, than how their own minds are constructed.
One might almost be content to leave them and their preference alone, on the ground that they are the best judges of the respective value of their own several commodities.
Great reason have I to be thankful, again I say, that I was suckled at the breast of Transcendentalism.

===Chinese===

这种关于形而上学充斥着学究气的议论，本该是这个时代引以为耻的陈腐落后观念，然而即便在今日，我们偶尔仍会碰到某些书评家，他们以令人动容的哀怨腔调向公众大吐苦水，宣称自己夜晚根本无法给妻子朗读希科克（Hickok）的《理性心理学》（*Rational Psychology*），只因这位博士那学究气的专门术语，使得该书即便是对那位才华横溢的女士而言亦宛若天书。

大体而言，我们可以稳妥地确立这样一条命题作为法则：先粗略浏览一本论述心智的著作，审视其在严整与系统性方面的总体特征，以辨明其作者既非愚顽之徒亦非草率之辈；随后，怀着赤诚公允平心静气地将其通读，你便会发觉，其专门术语密集的程度往往恰恰正比于其承载崭新与深邃真理的深厚底蕴。当然，这一点必须适用于那些依德·昆西（De Quincey）在其评述蒲柏（Pope）的批评文中所划分的、归属于“知识的文学”而非“力量的文学”的心智著作。那种正在松弛解构我们美国文学筋骨的心智懈怠习气——那种沉溺于当人们半睡半醒蜷在沙发上时便能不费吹灰之力自行流入耳畔的书卷；那种贪恋可以囫囵吞下且转瞬之间便能带来感官刺激的甜点佳肴的贪婪心态——正是这种习癖，剥夺了诸如希科克这般人物本应拥有的十分之九的读者。作为美国几乎唯一的真正形而上学家，或许亦是当今举世在世的最伟大者，足以与任何时代或国度的最坚毅深邃的思想家并驾齐驱的人物，他在自己的祖国所拥有的通晓且能鉴赏其著作的读者，其数量大概仅与毕达哥拉斯所拥有的密传门徒相当。我们委实有理由担忧：世人更热衷于探求棉布、干草耙以及凌驾于一切并囊括一切的金钱究竟是如何制造出来的，却远不愿探究他们自己的心智究竟是如何构筑生成的。

人们甚至几乎心满意足于任由他们去保留这种偏好，理由是他们大概最懂得衡量自己手中各项商品的各自价值吧。

我委实有莫大的理由再次满怀感恩：自己乃是吮吸着先验主义的乳汁成长起来的。

===Original===

I am doubtless not without sympathy in others when I say that the first moment when it flashed upon me how in the Reason might be found the laws and the essences of things, and that we were not confined for our knowledge to the mere ungrouped and unsettled appearances of the Sense, was like a revelation; it expanded and dignified the soul with a sudden access of glories such as no earthly kingship could give. At that moment spirit appeared to me for the first time something more than the hopeless bondslave of matter. For the sake of experiencing that feeling again in its full force of grand joyousness, I would like to exchange places with Locke, at the instant of his disembodiment, when he found out that he was mistaken. In having gone astray as a philosopher, he suddenly had all the more glorious surprise as a soul.
There was one question, however, which for a long time troubled me, but to which I at length got a satisfactory, although, perhaps, most men may disagree with me in the belief that it is a true answer. This is the question, How does the outer world ever become apparent to the spirit? I could see very easily how in the Reason the law conditioning an outer world might be found, but how did the appearances themselves become known? The manner of intercourse between matter and matter, between spirit and spirit, or between any two individualities of the same kind, was plain enough, or at least such an intercourse was reasonable. But with our views of matter and spirit, two existences in their very essence utterly dissimilar, how could they ever become tangent?
Take, for instance, such a case as this: I am hit by a stone. The thrill conveyed along its appropriate nerve runs up the brain, and here we trace its ultimate footprint on the material organism. Yet an infinitesimal instant more, and my mind has learned it, is moved to anger, and reasons for revenge or remedy. I could not see the connection by which the fact of the blow, however refined by its passage, was prolonged from matter into spirit. The books said that, on reaching the brain, the fact became a tertium quid, a third something, neither matter nor spirit, but so etherealized that the mind could read it. What, however, was that tertium quid?

===Chinese===

当我言及自己最初顿悟的那一刻——意识到在理性（Reason）之中竟能寻觅到万物的法则与本质，意识到我们的知识绝非仅仅禁锢于感官那些未经归类且飘忽不定的表象之上——宛若一道天启神示，无疑能博得诸多同道的共鸣；它以一种凡俗王权绝无法赋予的骤然降临的辉煌荣光，扩展并升华了灵魂的尊严。在那一刻，在我眼中，精神首次不再仅仅是物质那不可救药的屈辱奴隶。为了能够再次全身心体验那种充沛而宏阔的欢愉之感，我甚至甘愿在洛克脱离肉躯的那一瞬间与他互换位置——在那一刻，他赫然发觉自己昔日大错特错。正因作为哲学家步入了歧途，作为灵魂的他反而骤然收获了更为无比辉煌的惊愕震撼。

然而，曾有一个问题长期困扰折磨着我，尽管最终我寻获了一个令自己满意的答案——即便世人或许大多不会赞同其为真正的真理。这个问题便是：外部世界究竟是如何对精神显现呈现的？我极易看清在理性之中如何能够发掘出制约外部世界的法则，然而那些表象本身究竟是如何为人所知的？物质与物质之间、精神与精神之间，或同类的任意两个个体之间的交融神会方式，原本一清二楚，或者至少这种交融是合乎理性的。然而依照我们对物质与精神的认知，两者在其本质上截然不同，它们究竟是如何发生接触相切的？

举例而言，且看这样一个案例：我被一块石头击中。顺着对应神经传导的震颤直奔大脑，在彼处我们能够追踪到它在物质机体上留下的最终印迹。然而再经过无限微小的刹那，我的心智便已洞悉了此事，被激怒，并开始筹划复仇或救治。我却无法看清这一击打的事实——无论在其传递过程中被提炼得何等纤细精微——究竟是凭借何种纽带从物质延展延伸至精神之中的。典籍中写道，在抵达大脑之时，该事实演变转化为一种“第三中介物（tertium quid）”，某种既非物质亦非精神的第三种存在，但被空灵化至心智足以研读领会的程度。然而，那所谓的“第三中介物”究竟为何物？

===Original===

In the process of time, and by the aid of that ever to be blessed Transcendentalism which had helped me out of my earliest perplexity, I came to the conclusion that the tertium quid was a humbug, a metaphysical Mrs. Harris, upon whom the responsibility of all things impossible to be done or conceived was laid by psychological Mistresses Gamp. The answer which satisfied me was this: that there are only two kinds or modes of existence in the universe﻿—the one, self-conscious spirit; the other, the acts of such spirit. From these data arose such a theory of the universe as the following:
The Supreme Being, as Creator of all things, is ever in activity, according to certain eternal and universal laws of right and truth. Whatever else of self-conscious Being exists, came forth originally as an efflux from him, but is now in its will, though not for the continuance of its separate existence, independent of his direct action. As spirit, man is capable of communion with the supreme spirit. Since, however, spirit itself is in its very essence imperceptible to senses, the communion makes itself perceptible by appearances. These appearances, whose cause we call “matter,” are therefore, in reality, but the effects of spirit’s action upon spirit. In no sense, then, does any such thing as *dead matter* exist. It is God’s thinking felt by us.
If it shall be said that there is no difference between this and Pantheism, let me be allowed to show how the two systems differ toto caelo. I do not assert that matter is God. I say that the actor is God, and the effects of his action upon other spirit, which we call matter, are neither God, nor in any sense self-conscious. To make it clear, let my reader suppose himself striking a blow. He here appears as the self-conscious actor; yet how great an absurdity would it appear to him to call the blow itself after his name, or to attribute self-consciousness to it. He would say, The act of striking is an abstract idea, to which the other idea of self-consciousness can not be pertinent.
To carry out the parallel for further illustration, let us suppose this blow to fall upon the cheek of a bystander. The man struck would gain, from the effect produced on him, a pretty correct idea of the state of the striker’s feeling, notwithstanding he did not suppose that the blow was the striker, nor that it thought for itself.

===Chinese===

随着时光流逝，并在那曾经将我从最早的困惑中解救出来的、永受赞颂的先验主义的襄助之下，我得出了结论：所谓的“第三中介物”纯属一场骗局，不过是一位形而上学界的哈里斯太太（Mrs. Harris）——心理学界的干普太太（Mistress Gamp）们将一切无法施行或无法构想之事的责任，统统推卸赖在了她头上。令我心悦诚服的答案乃是：宇宙之中仅仅存在着两种存在类别或存在模态——其一，乃是具自知自识的精神；其二，则是此种精神的行动作为。立足于这些基石之上，生发出了如下这套宇宙理论：

至高存在（Supreme Being）作为万物的造物主，依照公义与真理的某些永恒且普世的法则，永恒处于作为运动之中。世间存在的其余一切具自知自识的存在，原初皆是作为流注自他体内涌现生发出来的，然而在其意志层面——尽管就其独立存在的维系而言并非如此——如今已独立于他的直接作为。作为精神，人类有能力同至高精神展开交融神会。然而，由于精神本身在其本质上对肉体感官而言是无法感知的，这种神会便通过种种表象令自身变得可以为人感知。因此，这些我们将起因称为“物质”的表象，在实质上无非是精神作用于精神之上的效应成果。由此观之，在任何意义上皆绝不存在所谓的“死物质（dead matter）”。它乃是被我们所感知领会到的神之沉思。

倘若有人声称这同泛神论（Pantheism）毫无二致，且容我阐明这两套体系究竟是如何有着云泥之别（toto caelo）。我绝非断言物质即是上帝。我是说行动的施动者是上帝，而他的作为施加于其余精神之上所产生的效应（我们称之为物质），既非上帝本身，亦在任何意义上皆不具备自知自识。为了阐明这一点，读者不妨设想自己挥出了一记重击。在此情境中，他显现为具自知自识的施动者；然而，倘若要将这一击本身冠以他的名号，或将自知自识归属于这一击本身，在他看来该是何等荒谬绝伦之事。他定会说：挥击的动作乃是一项抽象观念，自知自识的观念绝无法与之相提并论。

为了进一步类比阐发，且让我们设想这记重击落在了旁观者的面颊之上。挨打之人凭借自身所承受的作用效应，便能相当准确地领会把握施击者的情绪状态，尽管他绝不会臆想那一记击打即是施击者本人，亦绝不会认为那一记击打自身能够独立思考。

===Original===

Similarly in kind, let us suppose that the Deity is forever acting out through all the universe the principles of his infinite and righteous mind. By the effects of this action he becomes known to his spiritual creatures, and in reality manifests the state of his mind toward them. By such action, in its effect upon us known as matter, he attains the only incarnation of himself for reciprocal communion which could make him known.
I have said that this resolution of the problem of the Universe is the only one which ever satisfied me. The deductions which I made from it served to keep my own activity alive through many a day of suffering; and thus from it, in its satisfaction and its energizing, I received a double good. I will state some of the deductions.
Let me be permitted, for the sake of consonance with my theory, to speak, where accuracy is wanted, of matter, known in this light as the effect of the divine action, under the name of Force. I do not employ it in its mere mechanical sense, but as expressive of the manner of communion between two spiritual beings, to an extent metaphorically meaning something analogous to what in matter would be called the result of impingement.
1. In our bodily organism is one of the most cogent proofs of the Supreme goodwill toward us. By his own act he has insphered us within a force, the body, which not only resists many other forces and preserves its own integrity, but, what is of much greater importance, modifies our reception of knowledge from without, and blunts the acuteness of our action within to such an extent that truth does not come to us with a fatal shock, but gradually and softened, until we are able to bear it. Viewed as a counteractive force, the body is thus one of the highest proofs of God’s benignity, since, left in our present state of spiritual infancy without it, no lidless eyeball beneath a noonday sun might be more agonized. It is as much cause for thanksgiving as for aspiration to something clearer, that we now “see through a glass darkly.” Let us not repine, for there is a reason in these half opaque and tinged panes. A sun as consuming as he is wondrously glorious is shining just outside.
2. We may here find a further illustration of that which in the previous pages has been said of the symbolization, by every existence of the world, of some spiritual fact.

===Chinese===

同理类推，且让我们设想神明（Deity）正永恒不息地在全宇宙之中践行其无限且公义的心智原则。借由这一作为的种种效应，他令自身为人性属灵造物所知晓，并在实质上向他们昭示彰显了他对他们的心意状态。借由这一作为——其施加于我们之上的效应被我们称为物质——他达成了唯有如此才能使自己显露于世的、用于双向互通神会的唯一化身。

我已经说过，对宇宙这一难题的上述化解，乃是唯一曾令我心满意足的解答。我由此所推导出的诸多结论，支撑着我在无数个饱受痛苦折磨的日子里维系着自身的心智生机；因此，从其带来的欣慰与赋能之中，我斩获了双重裨益。且容我陈述其中的若干推论。

为了与我的理论保持协调一致，且容许我在需要精确表达之时，将依此视角所认识的作为神圣作为效应的物质，称之为“力（Force）”。我并非仅仅在机械意义上使用这一术语，而是用它来表达两个精神存在之间的神会方式，在某种程度上比喻着类似于物质世界中所谓的“撞击效应”之物。

1. 在我们的肉体机能之中，蕴含着至高存在对我们怀抱至善好意的最有力证据之一。借由他自身的作为，他将我们包裹笼罩在一股力量——肉躯——之中；这具身躯不仅能够抵御诸多其他外力以维系自身的完整，而且更重要的是，它调节修饰了我们对来自外部知识的接纳，并钝化弱化了我们内部作用的敏锐尖锐程度，其调节之深，使得真理降临于我们之时不至于带来致命的剧烈震荡，而是循序渐进、温和舒缓，直至我们足以承受。作为一股抵抗抗衡之力，肉体机能由此成为了神之慈悲的最崇高明证；因为倘若任由处于当下精神幼年期的我们失去肉躯，即便是在正午艳阳之下失去眼睑的眼球，其所承受的剧痛亦绝无法与之相比。我们如今“隔着毛玻璃管窥微光（see through a glass darkly）”，既是渴望更澄澈光明的因由，亦同样是满怀感恩的凭据。切莫抱怨叹息，因为在这些半透明且带有斑斓色泽的玻璃窗后自有深意：一轮既无比辉煌耀眼又足以将人吞噬焚尽的烈日，正悬照在窗门之外。

2. 在此，我们能够为前文数页中所论及的“世间每一种存在皆象征着某种精神事实”寻获进一步的阐释说明。

===Original===

The incarnation is as the essence; the universe is as it is because God lives as he lives. He is making himself felt in the effects of his communion with us.
A thousand times in the year do we hear it said that every plant is an evidence of God’s goodness; yet how much more amply, more nobly is this true than men generally suppose! Whatever of horror or deformity exists in the unconscious creation, is but the manifestation of Creative displeasure at our wrong; whatever of beauty (and how prodigally it is spread abroad!) is a testimony, rich with meaning, of that benevolence which mixes its displeasure with pity, and the return of wondrous good for an evil which is only less boundless. The continuance of Niagara, with its wealth of ennobling influences, is as speaking a proof to every man of God’s good feeling to him as the continuance of his life. In the millennium to which men are looking forward, how easily conceivable is it that by the literal fulfillment of our grandest prophecies and hopes; that an unblemished scenery, an illimitable luxuriance of greenness in the fields, an inspiration of beauty by every visible thing, may be the exponent of the gladness in the Great Heart above us at the restoration into perfectness of his filial race.
But our philosophy does not limit us to an analytic gaze upon the earth alone. The firmament, from our eyes onward in all directions forever, is full of stars. Some of these, perhaps all of them, there is reason to believe are peopled; but, granting that they roll on in utter loneliness, what of that? They are there, and as they are, because God is acting grandly, wisely, and righteously, and that it is all-satisfying to know. Even now they teach us lessons nightly, speaking both of Beauty and Truth. But what if they may be, also, carrying on their far-off orbits some incarnation of an attribute of God, which in our present state, we are not sufficiently strong to bear?
It is the characteristic of the written revelation to be comprehensive. Doubtless all of God is there in the germ; yet how many a line is drawn purposely in deep shadow! We are not ready for it yet.
The natural revelation, the universe, is in itself as comprehensive; but, since we can never see it all at once, to us it must necessarily be fragmentary.

===Chinese===

化身取决于本质；宇宙之所以如此这般呈现，乃因上帝依照他自身的方式而生息。他正借由同我们神会所产生的效应，令我们切实感知到他的存在。

一年之中我们成千上万次听闻人们说道：每一株植物皆是上帝仁善的明证；然而这一真理的宏阔与崇高，实则远超世人通常的想象！无意识造物之中所存在的一切可怖或畸形，无非是造物主对我们之过咎所怀不悦的彰显呈现；而世间所存在的一切美（且是何等慷慨挥洒地遍布天地！），则是那份将不悦同怜悯交织相融、以美妙之善回报那近乎无边之恶的仁慈的寓意深长之见证。尼亚加拉瀑布（Niagara）的长流不息及其所孕育的无尽崇高感化力，对每个人而言皆是昭示上帝对其怀抱善意的雄辩明证，一如其生命的延续不息。在世人所翘首以盼的千禧年里，借由我们最宏伟预言与期盼的字面兑现，是何等容易设想：一片毫无瑕疵的壮丽风光、原野上无边无际的苍翠繁茂、凡肉眼所见之物皆激发出的美感灵思，皆可能昭示着居于我们之上的至高浩瀚之心因其子嗣部族重归完美而萌生的无上欣悦。

然而，我们的哲学并未将我们禁锢于仅仅对大地投去分析性的凝视。穹苍天宇自我们眼前向四面八方永恒延展，繁星密布。我们有理由相信，其中的某些星辰——或许全部星辰——皆繁衍栖居着生灵；然而，纵使假定它们在彻底的孤寂中运转回旋，那又何妨？它们伫立在彼处，且依照当下的模样运转，乃因上帝正以宏大、智慧与公义的方式施行作为，知晓这一点便足以令人心满意足。即便在当下，它们每夜亦在向我们传授教诲，诉说着“美”与“真”。然而，倘若它们在遥远的天体轨道之上，亦承载着某种在当下状态中我们尚且脆弱得无法承受的神之属性的化身呢？

文字启示的特质在于其博大包罗。毫无疑问，上帝的全部奥秘皆以胚芽之态潜藏于其中；然而，又有多少行文字是蓄意勾勒在深沉的幽影之中啊！我们如今尚未做好接纳它们的准备。

自然启示——即宇宙本身——亦同样博大包罗；然而，由于我们绝无法一览无余地将其全盘尽收眼底，对我们而言，它便必然显现为支离破碎的片段。

===Original===

Thus we now have Earth to read from; yet when we are disembodied and purified﻿—when the incarnation through which the Divine is to come to us may with safety be made less gross than its form in the present matter, we shall learn through the stars, which have been kept waiting for us, sublimer and still sublimer truths of spirit throughout an ascending life.
Well may the man who, while his utmost gaze now catches them only as gleaming points, yet rejoices in the assurance of their significant harmony, break forth,
“O yet uninterrupted symbols, from afar I hail you as the promise of a truth which it is for Immortality to drink in! Beautiful, strange, yet not inexplicable; even now are ye beaming links of that chain which binds me to Deity; ye shall hereafter draw me close to his presence in a grander communion. Await me brightly while I calmly long for you.”
In the closest circle of earthly fellowship wherein I have known what it is for heart to be knit with heart, it has ever been the beautiful custom to write the dead, who, though absent, were still one with the living brotherhood, under this title,

> “Qui fuerunt, sed nunc ad astra.”

===Chinese===

因此，我们如今拥有大地供我们研读领会；然而，当我们摆脱肉躯、洗涤净化的时刻到来之时——当神圣向我们走来所依凭的化身得以安全地剥除其在当下物质中的粗粝形态之时——我们将透过那些一直在此静候我们的繁星，在不断跃升的生命旅程中体悟关于精神的愈发崇高、愈发壮丽的真理。

目睹此情此景，哪怕目力所及只能将它们捕捉为闪烁微茫的光点，却因确信其深具意义的和谐交响而满怀欣悦之人，尽可情不自禁地高呼赞颂：

“啊，尚绵延不绝的象征符记啊，我自遥远之地向你们致意，视你们为唯有永生方能饮尽的真理之许诺！美丽、奇异，却非不可索解；即便在当下，你们亦是系结我与神明的灿烂链环；在未来的岁月里，你们终将在一场更为恢弘的神会中将我引领至他的身旁。在明澈的光晕中静候我吧，正当我心如止水地渴盼着你们。”

在我所深知的、心与心紧密相连的人世手足情谊最亲密的圈子里，向来流传着这样一种优美动人的风尚习俗：给那些虽已离席长逝、却依然同在世兄弟骨肉相连的故人题写下这般名号：

> “彼等曾居于此，今已归于星海（Qui fuerunt, sed nunc ad astra）。”

===Original===

How grand a meaning may there be in this!
3. Upon the ground that all knowledge through sense may be resolved into the idea of force, there are some reasons for supposing that this force may in itself be simple, and only varied by its approach to the soul through the differently modifying organs. In fine, that sight, hearing, touch, taste, and smell may be effects, to speak after the common nomenclature, of the same object, or one grand effect divided into several by transmission.
An inspection of the analogies of science must convince us that this proposition, if not *a priori* necessarily true, is, at any rate, extremely probable. The progress of philosophical research is invariably from the complex to the simple. The myriad phenomena of chemistry are all traceable to the action and reaction in various combinations of a very limited number of elements; these elements are still farther resolvable in their composition into still fewer and more ethereal bodies. In the same way, all the mechanical operations are due to differing applications of six motors; and these, by still further analysis, arrange themselves under the head of physical force. These are but two instances out of the multitude which prove the great law of simplification by research.
In many a field of inquiry the philosopher has reduced the agents effective for a given result to two or three; the next step would bring him to the all-comprehensive unity; but no, that step can not be taken, for nature here so suddenly subtilizes the springs of her activity, that she may float just before the face of her hierophant, and laugh invisibly at baffled microscope and hypothesis. Yet enough is known in all departments of investigation to prove that the tendency of discovery is invariably from the vast periphery of facts inward to one single central law.
Yet let us not leave the theory of the all-comprehensive oneness of sense to base its plausibility upon a general analogy. We are able to particularize. What reason, then, have we, from known facts, to suppose all the senses directly referable to force? A brief analysis will discover most of the evidence we have.
And, 1st: Touch, simply considered as the organ for determining the hardness, weight, and form of bodies. The two former will be seen to be directly resolvable into force, viz.

===Chinese===

这其中蕴含着何等宏阔高迈的深意啊！

3. 立足于一切经由感官获取的知识皆可化约归结为“力”之观念的基石之上，我们有若干理由推断：这股力量本身或许是单纯无差别的，仅仅因其经由不同器官的修饰调节抵达灵魂，方才呈现出千差万别。简而言之，依世俗的通用命名法而言，视觉、听觉、触觉、味觉与嗅觉，或许皆是同一客体的效应，或是某一宏大效应在传递过程中被分流拆解为若干细支。

对科学类比的审视必然会令我们笃信：这一命题纵然并非先验（a priori）必然为真，至少亦具有极高的可能性。哲学研究的演进，无一例外皆是从繁复走向单纯。化学中无数纷繁复杂的现象，皆可追溯至极少数有限元素的各种组合作用与反作用；而这些元素的构成，更可进一步化约归结为数量更少、更为空灵虚无的实体。同理，一切机械运转皆归因于六种原动机的不同应用；而这些原动机经过进一步分析，亦统统归入物理力的范畴之下。在浩如烟海的例证之中，这两者不过是证明通过研究实现简化这一伟大规律的冰山一角。

在诸多探索领域之中，哲学家已将达成某项特定结果的有效因子削减至两三个；再迈出一步，便能将他引向涵盖万象的终极归一；然而且慢，那一步却绝无法跨出，因为大自然在此处以极其骤然的机巧将其运动枢机精微化，以至于她得以在大祭司眼前飘然而过，暗中嘲笑着束手无策的显微镜与虚妄假说。然而，在各个调查门类中所知晓的已然足够充分，足以证明探索发现的趋势无一例外皆是从庞大外围的事实向内收敛汇聚至单一的核心法则。

然而，我们且莫让这种关于感官包罗万象同一性的理论仅仅立足于宽泛笼统的类比之上。我们能够逐一详述。那么，从已知的事实出发，我们究竟有何理由推断所有感官皆可直接归结为力呢？一番简短的剖析便能揭示我们所拥有的大部分证据。

首先，第1点：触觉（Touch），单纯将其视作测定物体的坚硬度、重量与形状的器官。前两者显而易见可直接化约归结为力，即——

===Original===

, the force of resistance, in the one case particularized as cohesion, in the other as gravitation. The distinction of form may be also comprehended as an idea of force by the following statement. I move my hand in all directions in the plane of the horizon, and, finding it everywhere resisted *by an equal force* from below, say, “This is a flat surface.”
The resistance, in another instance, occurs in a different direction, and I express this fact by saying sphere, cone, ellipsoid, etc., as the case may be.
2nd: Sight. It is, no doubt, well known to many of my readers that, in modern times, two theories have obtained upon the action of light, or, more properly, its origination. Both of them arose or were resuscitated from antiquity in the seventeenth century, but that which bears the name of Huygens is by a few years the earlier. This philosopher held that all luminous bodies are in a state of almost infinitely rapid, though infinitesimally small vibration; that this vibration propagates itself in all directions with an undulatory motion through an exceedingly subtle and elastic fluid, known as ether, which fills all space; that these undulations, impinging against any material body, bound back, or, in usual parlance, are reflected to the eye, and, striking upon the retina, give through the optic nerve, of which it is an expansion, the sensation of sight.
The second theory is that of Newton, who supposed that luminous bodies are continually giving off infinitesimal radiant particles, which, through the ethereal medium, impinge upon the eye in the same manner and with the same effect as the light-waves of Huygens’ theory.
The former hypothesis (viz., Huygens’) is that at this day entertained by the majority of savants, but the discovered laws of optics accord equally well with either. No further dissertation is necessary to show that in either case the conception of sight is resolvable into the idea of a perceived *force*.
3rd: Hearing. Upon this sense there is certainly no need of enlarging, it being universally known that sound is the offspring of vibration, and therefore a *force*, subject in its transmission, reflection, etc., to laws precisely analogous to those of light, modified merely by the nature of the medium, viz., air or grosser bodies, through which it travels, in contradistinction to the infinitely subtle ether which propagates light.

===Chinese===

——阻力（force of resistance），在前一种情形中具体表现为内聚力（cohesion），在后一种情形中具体表现为重力（gravitation）。而形状的界分，亦可借由如下陈述被理解为一种力的观念：我在水平面上向各个方向挥动双手，发觉它在各处皆承受着来自下方一股均等之力的抵御，于是说道：“这是一处平面。”

而在另一种情形中，阻力发生在不同的方向上，我便依据具体情况将其表达为球体、圆锥体、椭球体等等。

第2点：视觉（Sight）。毫无疑问，诸多读者皆熟知：在近世，关于光的作用（或者更确切地说，光的起源）曾流传着两套理论。两套理论皆萌生于17世纪或在17世纪从古代死灰复燃，然而冠以惠更斯（Huygens）之名的那一套要早生数年。这位哲学家认为：所有发光体皆处于一种近乎无限快速、却又无限微小的振动状态之中；这种振动借由充斥全宇宙空间的某种极其精微且具备弹性的流体（即以太，ether），以波动的方式向四面八方传播；这些波纹在撞击到任何物质实体之后反弹折返，或者依寻常说法，反射至眼眸之中，撞击在视网膜上，并借由视神经（视网膜即视神经的延展）传导生发出视觉。

第二套理论乃是牛顿（Newton）的假说，他设想发光体不断释放出无限微小的辐射微粒，它们借由以太介质撞击眼眸，其方式与效应同惠更斯理论中的光波完全一致。

前一种假说（即惠更斯假说）乃是当今绝大多数学者所奉行的学说，然而业已发掘的光学法则与这两者皆严丝合缝。无须进一步详述便足以证明：在上述任意一种假说中，视觉的概念皆可化约归结为一种被感知到的“力”的观念。

第3点：听觉（Hearing）。对于这一感官，自无须赘言多谈，因为世人皆知声音乃是振动的产物，因而本身便是一种“力”，在其传播、反射等方面受制于同光线完全类似的法则，仅仅因其穿透行进的介质性质（即空气或更粗糙的实体）而有所修饰，以区别于传播光线的无限精微的以太。

===Original===

There is, however, one analogy upon which we may dwell for a short time, which would seem greatly to strengthen the general theory that all sensations are, in their essential agency, one.
The relationship between light and sound does not terminate in the fact, of itself sufficiently striking, that they both obey similar conditions of transmission and reflection. True, they each pass to the human organ, not by one unbroken leap, but by a series of waves. Literally, lightning no more *darts* upon the eye than the faintest beam of dawn; thunder comes undulating to the ear as truly as the softest sigh; and the light cast upon us from a mirror is only an echo through ether instead of air.
But there is a far more intricate affiliation between them. In the very possibilities of their existence they are the same. Every ray of light can be comprehended within the range of seven radical colors and the combinations of them. This law of but seven possible colors is not an accident, but a primeval and necessary accompaniment of the manner of transmission.
Every possible sound likewise lies between the two termini of a gamut whose number of root sounds is seven, and this septenary law of sound is as necessary as that of light.
The universality with which these laws are practically known by means of the prism and the octave, take off, as is the case in so many other habitual mysteries, the edge of our legitimate wonder. Yet when, for a moment, we reflect calmly upon the fact that we may analyze light of any possible kind with the most rigid scrutiny without adding a single principal color to a fixed range of seven; that we may utter any conceivable sound without escaping from the same mystic boundaries; that in both cases our only changes must be rung by reduplication or blending within those adamantine gamut walls; when we reflect, I say, on these two truths, each fit food in itself for wonder, and find that in fact they are but one truth, and that a characteristic of sensations which we have always treated as essentially different, we shall have reason to confess, with amazement, a far more intimate union between sight and hearing than any of outer coincidence. Indeed, excepting the before-mentioned difference, which their several media of travel impress upon them, philosophy can not find a mark of distinction between sight and sound.

===Chinese===

然而，存在着这样一则类比，我们可以略作停留品味，它似乎极大强化了“一切感知在其本质机制上皆归为一”的总体理论。

光与声之间的亲缘纽带，绝非仅仅止步于两者皆遵从类似的传播与反射条件这一本身便足够引人注目的事实。确凿无疑的是，它们各自传入人类器官，皆非凭借孤立不绝的单次跃迁，而是经由一系列波纹。从字面上讲，闪电射入眼帘的方式绝不比拂晓最微弱的一缕晨光更为“迅疾疾驰”；雷鸣起伏翻滚涌入耳畔的真切程度，丝毫不亚于最柔和的轻叹；而镜面投射在我们身上的光芒，无非是通过以太而非空气所传递的回响罢了。

然而，在它们之间存在着一种远为精微深邃的血缘关联。在它们赖以存在的一切潜质可能之中，它们是完全同一的。每一缕光线皆可囊括在七种基色（seven radical colors）及其交织组合的范畴之内。这仅仅可能存在七种颜色的法则绝非偶然巧合，而是其传播机制原初且必然的伴生规律。

同理，每一种可能的声音亦坐落在一个音域的两端极限之间，其基音数量恰恰亦是七种；而关于声音的这套“七元法则（septenary law）”，一如光的法则那般不可或缺。

借由棱镜与八度音阶，这些法则在实践中为人所共知的普遍性，正如诸多其他早已习以为常的奥秘一样，磨平了我们本应怀抱的合理惊叹之情。然而，当我们稍加平心静气地反思这一事实：我们无论以何等严苛的审视去分析任何一种可能存在的光线，皆绝无法在那固定的七种基色范围之外增添哪怕一种主要色彩；我们无论发出何种可以设想的声音，皆绝无法挣脱完全相同的神秘藩篱；在两种情形中，我们所能实现的全部变化，皆唯有在那坚不可摧的音阶壁垒之内通过重叠复奏或交织融合来达成；我说，当我们反思这两项真理（其每一项本身皆足以激发起无尽的惊叹），并赫然发觉它们实则仅为同一项真理，且是一直被我们视作本质迥异的种种感知的共同特征之时，我们便有充沛的理由惊愕地承认：在视觉与听觉之间，存在着一种远比任何外在巧合都更为深邃密切的同盟归一。事实上，除了前文提及的因其各自穿透的媒介所赋予的差异之外，哲学在视觉与声音之间根本找寻不出任何界分的标志。

===Original===

How strong a claim to interior oneness this law of seven bestows can be fully felt only by realizing how essential a law it is. So essential is it that probably, in the whole universe, it may be impossible to find a complete range of any operations which does not, in its internal nature, submit to it. I say this perfectly aware that there are insuperable obstacles, while we enjoy no more than our present development of mind, to proving this to a logical certainty. Yet the vast probability which appears to me in the proposition rests upon one fact which I have never seen noticed in connection with these senses. Doubtless it has been noticed, however, for from time immemorial the significancy of the number seven has employed the researches of philosophers and theologians. The fact is this: In the Divine philosophy of Creation, which is, at the same time, the most reverend also for age, there is a stress laid upon the importance of this number as exponent of some law of completion, of perfectness, which, unless it be granted deeply significant, can be treated upon no middle ground between that and a puerility partaking of imposture. The *seventh* day as the one whose advent expressly witnessed the completion of the Kosmos (whatever of length we may give to the days of the creation); the impress of some secret import upon *seven*, by countless ceremonial symbols, inculcated to that people who, during the whole period of the Theocracy, held more direct communion with the Divine source of all Truth than any nation before or since; the constant recurrence, in the Word, of prophetic uses of the number, and such a phrase as this: “Wisdom hath builded her house; she hath hewn out her *seven* pillars:” all these seem to indicate, beyond the possibility, in my mind at least, of conceiving the contrary possible, that this number is a fundamental law of perfectness in the Universe.
As such, therefore, and comprehending under its rule the two senses of sight and hearing, it proves a oneness in their essential conditions which seems irrefutable. In some of the more intensely awakened hashish states, there was a great light thrown upon this subject, but, with many other views gained like it, through symbolization, on my return to the natural state it passed away from my mind forever.
4. Smell.

===Chinese===

这套“七之法则”所赋予的内在同一性的强有力主张，唯有通过领悟其究竟何等不可或缺，方能获得全然的体悟。它是如此根本，以至于在全宇宙之中，或许极难寻觅到任何一套在其内在本质上不归顺臣服于它的完整运行范畴。我说这番话时，深知在人类心智当下所达致的局限水平之下，欲以逻辑确定性去彻底证实它尚存在着不可逾越的鸿沟。然而，在我眼中这一命题所展现出的巨大可能性，乃是立足于一个我从未见人在论及这些感官时加以注意的事实之上。然而，毫无疑问它早就为人所察觉，因为自远古以来，数字“七”的深邃意义便一直吸引着哲学家与神学家的探究。这一事实便是：在关于创世的神圣哲学之中（它同时亦是因岁月沉淀而最受尊崇的哲学），极力强调了这一数字作为某种圆满、完备法则之体现的重要性；除非承认其蕴含着至深的深意，否则人们在深具意义与兼具骗术意味的稚拙把戏之间，便绝无任何折中的中间地带可言。第七日，作为其降临明确见证了宇宙天地（Kosmos）告成圆满的日子（无论我们赋予创世的诸日以何等漫长的时间尺度）；通过数不胜数的仪式符号将某种隐秘深意烙印在“七”之上，灌输传授给那群在神权统治的全盛时期比古往今来任何民族都更为直接地与一切真理的神圣源头相通神会的子民；圣道经典之中，对该数字预言性用途的反复涌现，以及诸如“智慧建造房屋，凿成七根柱子”这般的经文词句：凡此种种，似乎皆在昭示——至少在我心智所能构想的相反可能之外——这一数字乃是全宇宙完备圆满的根本法则。

因此，作为此种法则，并将视觉与听觉这两大感官囊括在其统辖之下，它雄辩地证明了两者在本质条件上的归一性，而这种归一性看似坚不可摧。在大麻激发出的一些更为强烈的觉醒状态中，曾有一道耀眼的明光投射于这一课题之上；然而，正如借助象征符号所斩获的诸多其他洞见一样，随着我重返日常状态，它便从我的脑海中彻底湮灭消逝了。

4. 嗅觉（Smell）。

===Original===

Within the last ten years an attempt has been made by some Frenchman of speculative mind, whose name I forget, to determine if this sense a septenary gamut also, in which the only two tones that have not escaped me, to the best of my knowledge, were citron and rose. If the natural existence of such a gamut could be accurately determined, it would be a great auxiliary, certainly, to our argument; but I fear that our knowledge of the catalogue and relations of all possible odors is so very imperfect as to make the research only a fanciful recreation. From the great variety of the objects, and the lack of scientific delicacy of the sense of smell, it is a very difficult one to deal with. He who investigates it through its own instrumentality, which, of course, is the only possible method for an inductive science, is very much at the disadvantage of him who should try to dissect an animalcule with his finger nail.
Yet there is, even with such obstacles in the way, a possibility of proving odor ultimately resolvable into that *force* which we have discovered as the common idea of the preceding senses. I have held the opinion, whether original with me or not I can not say, that odor, like light and sound, may be propagated by undulations; if not as the only mode, at least as one of two modes, the other of which is immediate chemical action upon the organ. As an argument in favor of this, I would instance the grain of musk, which, without losing weight at all appreciably, will for years render the room in which it is kept intolerable to its enemies.
But, granting that the chemical action is its only one, this fact, so far from precluding the idea of *force* which we seek to make general, only illustrates it. The very chemical action is itself a force. As an example, notice the effect of some such odorous agent as makes its effect particularly marked; let us say hellebore, which, when smelled, causes odic action of the nerve, in some cases only less powerful than that appropriate to galvanism. The flower of the catalpa produces a similar effect upon myself, sufficiently severe to cause very troublesome bleeding; and I know several persons affected in like manner by the carnation pink and eglantine.
5. Taste. The theory supported by some physicists upon the operation of this very little scientifically understood sense is something such as this.

===Chinese===

在过去的十年里，某位富有思辨心智的法国人（其姓名我已淡忘）曾试图探明这一感官是否亦存在着一套七度音阶，据我所知，其中未从我记忆中溜走的仅有两种音调：香橼与玫瑰。倘若此类音阶的天然存在能够获得精确判定，毫无疑问将对我们的论证构成极大的助力；然而我唯恐我们对所有可能气味的编目及其相互关系的认知尚且极不完备，以至于该项研究只能沦为一种耽于幻想的消遣。鉴于气味客体的纷繁多样，加之嗅觉本身匮乏科学层面的敏锐精微，使得它成为了极难对付的一门感官。企图凭借其自身的器官工具去探究它的人（当然，这对于一门归纳科学而言乃是唯一可行之法），其不利境地极像一个企图用自己的手指甲去解剖一只微型极微虫的人。

然而，即便面临着此类阻碍，依然存在着证明气味最终亦可化约归结为我们在前述感官中所发掘出的那股共通之“力”的可能。我始终抱持这样一种观点（是否为我首创我不敢断言）：气味与光和声音一样，亦可能是经由波动传播的；即便这并非唯一的传播模态，至少亦是两种模态之一，而另一种则是对感官器官的直接化学作用。作为支持这一点的论据，我愿以一粒麝香为例：它在重量上几乎未发生任何可以察觉的损耗，却能在长达数年的时间里，令陈放它的居室对那些厌恶麝香之人而言变得难以忍受。

然而，纵使假定化学作用是其唯一的机制，这一事实非但绝未排除我们试图将其普遍化的“力”之观念，反而恰恰阐释印证了它。化学作用本身即是一种力。举例而言，且看某些气味极其显著的气味媒介的效应：譬如嚏根草（hellebore），当人们嗅闻它时，会引发神经的俄德力（odic）反应，在某些情形下其剧烈程度仅次于伽伐尼电击。梓树（catalpa）的花朵对我自身亦能产生类似的效应，其剧烈程度足以引发极其棘手的鼻衄流血；我亦知晓有数人同样会对香石竹与野蔷薇（eglantine）产生类似的剧烈反应。

5. 味觉（Taste）。关于这一在科学上知之甚少的感官的运作机制，某些物理学家所支持的理论大体如下。

===Original===

The tongue, upon a foundation of muscular fibre, carries a nervous membrane, not wholly smooth even in the most delicate species, but bristling, more or less compactly, with highly sensitive minute nervous tufts, known to physiology under the name of “papillae,” literally, “little teats,” from their peculiar form. Sapid substances being dissolved by the saliva, and thus resolved into their ultimate particles, in the form of these particles penetrate the papillae. By something analogous to an exquisitely-refined sense of touch, these papillae detect the peculiar form characteristic of every ultimate particle of the given sapid substance, and thus define it as a certain taste.
If this be the correct explanation of the taste-phenomena, they resolve themselves into a perceived force of form, and thus come within our law. But I imagine that the operation is still more subtle; and that in every substance possessing sapidity, there is, producing the sensation, a force by itself, possessing as true an individuality as the electric, and in each case bearing a specific characteristic which gives it its peculiar taste. Perhaps it may be akin to the galvanic fluid. This seems to be suggested by the result of an experiment very easily made, viz., placing a circle of zinc upon one side of the tongue and of copper on the other, when the curious possibility will be manifest of actually “tasting galvanism.”
6. Feeling. I have made this distinction between feeling and touch for the reason that, although their sensations may be propagated along the same sets of nerves, the strongly-marked difference in nature between the facts which they separately apprehend renders it more philosophical to treat them apart. By feeling is meant here the sense of heat and its absence, pain of all kinds, and the sensuous pleasure not included under previously analyzed senses. In the latter part of this category, for instance, are included sexual gratification, the soothing effect of manipulation, whatever it may be styled, mesmeric or otherwise, and preeminently the exhilaration of narcotics and other stimuli.
The only argument which I shall adduce to prove the comprehension of the feeling-phenomena within the general idea of force will be simply to call to my reader’s mind the fact that all such phenomena are spasmodic.

===Chinese===

舌头在肌纤维的基础之上覆盖着一层神经膜，即便在最娇嫩的个体身上亦绝非全然光滑，而是或疏或密地布满了极其敏感微小的神经丛，在生理学上被称为“乳头（papillae）”——因其独特的形态，字面原意即“小乳头”。具味道的滋味物质被唾液溶解，从而化解为其终极微粒，以这些微粒的形态渗入乳头之中。借由类似于某种极其精微考究的触觉机制，这些乳头探测出所给滋味物质之每一终极微粒的特有形态特征，从而将其界定为某种特定的味道。

倘若这正是对味觉现象的正确解释，那么它们自身便化解归结为一种被感知到的“形态之力”，从而纳入了我们的法则之中。然而我设想其运作机制还要更为精微：在每一种具备滋味的物质之中，皆存在着一股独立生发出该感知的力量本身，它如同电荷一样拥有真切的个异性，且在每一种情形中皆携带着赋予其特有味道的特定属性。或许它同伽伐尼流体（galvanic fluid）一脉相承。一项极其容易施行的实验结果似乎便昭示了这一点，即：将一枚锌片放置在舌头的一侧，另一侧放置一枚铜片，此时便能赫然展现出真真切切“品尝到伽伐尼电味”的奇妙可能。

6. 体感知觉（Feeling）。我之所以在体感知觉与触觉之间做出这种界分，乃是因为：尽管两者的感知或许是沿着同一组神经传导的，但它们各自所捕捉到的事实在本质上有着鲜明深刻的差异，这使得将它们分门别类加以探讨显得更为符合哲学之道。在此处，体感知觉是指对热及其匮乏（寒冷）的感觉、各种形式的疼痛，以及未包含在前述剖析感官之中的肉体感官欢愉。譬如在后一类别的范畴中，便囊括了性满足、推拿抚触的舒缓抚慰效应（无论将其称之为动物磁气催眠术还是其他名目），以及最显著的麻醉剂与其他兴奋剂的狂喜振奋。

为了证明体感知觉现象囊括在力的普遍观念之内，我所要引述的唯一论据，便是唤醒读者去铭记这样一个事实：一切此类现象皆具有痉挛抽搐性（spasmodic）。

===Original===

Their idea is that of an injected energy of motion, manifest not only in the nerve, but in the brain, by contraction or relaxation or both, or the alternation of the two states of either.
Having endeavored, as briefly as an analysis at all satisfactory would permit, to test the truth of my theory with respect to each division of the sense, let me, in a few words, sum up the substance of that which has been sought to be proved.
It is this. That the soul in itself is capable of receiving all the impressions of all the senses from the action of the object which produces an impression upon a single sense; that in the bodily organs only and the media of transmission, which are relevant to the organs alone, lies the necessity for a divisory action; and, finally, as a consequence of these propositions, that the soul, either wholly freed from its present gross body, or so awakened, by any cause, as to be partially independent of the intervention of the corporeal organs, may behold the manifold impression from an object which now gives it only the fractional, thus seeing, hearing, smelling, tasting, and feeling in the most exquisite degree the thing which, in the state of bodily dominance, was the source of but one of these.
An opinion similar to this was held by Coleridge; and I can not but believe that it was suggested to him by some intimation of its truth which he received while in the exaltation of opium. Certainly there is no corroboration greater than he might have thus acquired for it, if the effect of that drug ever reached with him the intensity which hashish reached with me. By evidence of the most startling character was I repeatedly, while using the indulgence, put beyond all doubt upon the point. Indeed, at this day it lies before me in the light of as distinct a certainty as any fact of my being. Because, from the very nature of its source, I could not transfer that certainty, in kind, to the mind of my reader, I have made the attempt to approximate it by the preceding argument, not because I felt at all the need of strengthening myself in the faith.
As, some distance back, I have referred to my own experience upon the subject, asserting my ability at times to *feel sights*, *see sounds*, etc., I will not attempt to illustrate the present discussion by a narrative of additional portions of my own case. It might be replied to me, “Ah!

===Chinese===

它们的观念乃是一种注入的运动能量，不仅表现在神经之中，亦借由收缩或舒张或两者兼具、或是两者的交替轮转而表现在大脑之中。

在力求以尽量简明扼要且差强人意的剖析去针对感官的各个门类检验我的理论真伪之后，且容我用寥寥数语，概括提炼前文试图证实的实质核心。

那便是：灵魂自身完全有能力从对单一感官产生印记的客体作用之中，接纳汲取一切感官的全部印记；分流拆解作用的必然性，仅仅存在于肉体器官以及仅仅与器官相关的传播介质之中；最终，作为这些命题的必然结论：灵魂无论是彻底摆脱其当下的粗粝肉躯，还是因任何缘由被深度唤醒以至于在某种程度上独立于肉体器官的介入干预，皆能从如今仅仅赋予其残缺碎片的客体之中，窥见其纷繁多样的全息印记，从而以最精微至极的程度去观瞧、聆听、嗅闻、品尝并感知那在肉体主导状态下仅仅充当其中单一感知来源的客体。

柯勒律治（Coleridge）曾抱持过与此类似的见解；我不禁深信，这乃是他沉浸于鸦片亢奋之中所捕获的关于该真理的某种启示神会所赋予他的灵感。毫无疑问，倘若该药物对他产生的效应曾达致大麻对我所达致的剧烈程度，他断不可能获取比这更伟大的印证了。在沉溺于该药剂期间，我凭借最令人惊愕的铁证屡次三番在这座要害之上彻底祛除了全部怀疑。事实上，时至今日，它在我眼前昭然若揭，其确凿无疑的程度丝毫不亚于关于我自身存在的任何事实。由于源头本身的特质，我无法将这种确凿性原原本本地迁移灌输至读者的心智之中，我这才试图借助前文的论证去无限逼近它，而绝非因为我自身感到有任何强化这一信念的需求。

鉴于我在不久之前曾提及自身在这一课题上的亲身体验，宣称自己有时能够“感知景象”、“看见声音”等等，我在此便不再试图通过叙述自身案例的额外部分来阐释眼下的讨论了。人们或许会对我说：“啊！

===Original===

yes, all very likely; but probably you are an exception to the general rule; nobody else might be affected so.” This was said to me quite frequently when, early in the hashish life, I enthusiastically related the most singular phenomena of my fantasië.
But there is no such thing true of the hashish effects. Just as inevitably as two men taking the same direction, and equally favored by Providence, will arrive at the same place, will two persons of similar temperament come to the same territory in hashish, see the same mysteries of their being, and get the same hitherto unconceived facts. It is this characteristic which, beyond all gainsaying, proves the definite existence of the most wondrous of the hashish-disclosed states of mind. The realm of that stimulus is no vagary; it as much exists as England. We are never so absurd as to expect to see insane men by the dozen all holding to the same hallucination without having had any communication with each other.
As I said once previously, after my acquaintance with the realm of witchery had become, probably, about as universal as anybody’s, when I chanced to be called to take care of someone making the experiment for the first time (and I always was called), by the faintest word, often by a mere look, I could tell exactly the place that my patient had reached, and treat him accordingly. Many a time, by some expression which other bystanders thought ineffably puerile, have I recognized the landmark of a field of wonders wherein I had traveled in perfect ravishment. I understood the symbolization, which they did not.
Particularly was this the case in the hashish experiment of a friend of mine, made not three months ago, spontaneously on his part, and unknown to me until I was “sent for.” Not only was it for ecstasy and wonderful phenomena the most remarkable I ever had the care of, but so clear a light did it shed on the investigation of the few preceding pages, that I will give it here in place of anything additional of my own, which, as I have said, I will not give.
B﻿—﻿—, this friend of mine, for four hours supposed that he was in heaven. Infinite leagues below him he heard the old, remembered bells of the world, and their sound, as it came floating, diminished up through the immense sky beneath him, seemed the only tie which bound him to anything not celestial.

===Chinese===

是啊，这一切皆大有可能；但你大概只是普遍规律之外的一个特例吧；换作旁人断不可能受到这般影响。”早在大麻生涯的初期，当我满腔热忱地向人讲述自己幻想世界中最奇绝的现象时，这类话语我便屡见不鲜地听过。

然而对于大麻的效应而言，这种论调绝非事实。正如两个走在同一方向上且同样得蒙天眷庇佑之人必然会抵达同一地点一样，两个性情相仿之人亦必然会在大麻之中踏入同一片疆域，窥见其存在深处完全相同的奥秘，并获取此前从未构想过的完全相同的事实。正是这一特质，无可辩驳地证明了由大麻所揭示的最神奇心智状态的确凿存在。那一刺激所构筑的疆域绝非虚妄的空想；它的存在一如英格兰那般确凿。我们绝不至于荒唐到期盼看到成打的疯子在彼此毫无交流沟通的前提下、竟然全都维系着完全相同的幻觉。

正如我此前曾提及的那样，在我对那一魔幻领域的熟稔程度大概已变得与任何人一般普遍深厚之后，每逢我碰巧被请去照料某位初次尝试这项实验之人时（而我向来皆会被请去），只需凭着最微弱的一言半语，往往仅仅凭借一个眼神，我便能精确断定我的看护对象究竟抵达了何等境地，并据此对其施加照护。许多次，借由旁人视作荒唐稚拙的某句言辞，我便辨识出了我昔日曾沉浸在无上狂喜之中漫步涉足的那片奇迹之境的界标。我参透了其中的象征意象，而旁人却茫然不解。

我的某位友人在不到三个月前所做的一次大麻实验，尤为鲜明地印证了这一点——这项实验完全是他自发施行的，且在我被“请去”之前我对此一无所知。就出神狂喜与神奇现象而言，这不仅是我所照料过最非凡卓越的一例，而且它对前文数页的探究投射下了如此清晰透彻的明光，以至于我愿在此将其娓娓道来，以替代前文所述的我自身不愿再多加铺陈的经历。

我的这位友人B——，整整四个小时皆坚信自己置身于天堂之中。在脚下无尽无垠的万里深渊之下，他听闻了尘世间古老而熟悉难忘的钟声；那钟声自身下浩瀚辽阔的天宇飘摇回荡、渐渐微弱地传入耳畔，似乎构成了系结他与任何非属天国之物之间的唯一纽带。

===Original===

As I sat by the side of the sofa on which he was lying, and held his hand for a greater part of the time, I became a convert to all the most marvelous articles of the mesmeric creed. The connection which his peculiar state of sensitiveness had established between us, made us, for all purposes of sensation and perception, wholly one. I was able to follow him through all his ecstatic wanderings, to see what he saw, feel what he felt, as vividly as it is possible without myself having taken hashish. This, however, as you will say, was nothing wonderful. It might have happened, and no doubt, in part, did happen, from my former thorough acquaintance with all such states.
But the connection did not end here. I drank a glass of water, and B. felt it as distinctly as if he had taken it himself. He experienced the spasm of the muscles of the throat, which always accompanies drinking in the hashish state, so vividly that he really supposed he was drinking himself, and implored me to give him no more water.
For another person in the room he had always felt strong sympathies; they were now developed to an extent most surprising. This person had a habit, when in a brown study, of industriously rubbing his forehead after a fashion painful to look upon. Suddenly I heard B. exclaim, “Oh, Bob, stop thinking! stop thinking! you don’t know how it distresses my head!” My eyes had been upon B. all the time; his own had not once been opened; how could he have known that his friend was thinking? I looked around, and lo! Bob, in medio brown-studio, polishing his forefront with the usual assiduity. Merely by the sympathy between them B. had known it all. This may be laughed at, but, if necessary, I would willingly file my affidavit that B., with his outer eyes, had seen nothing for half an hour previous. I had not taken my eyes from his face once during that time.
But I will go on to the facts which more immediately bear on my theory. While, as I have said, he had not the remotest consciousness of the place in which he really was, he still conversed freely with us on the basis of his celestial locale.
To him, we all seemed to be together “in excelsis.” Naturally he was a loving and gentle spirit; this characteristic the upper atmosphere brought out more fully.

===Chinese===

当我在他横卧的沙发旁坐下、在大部分时间里紧紧握着他的手时，我沦为了动物磁气催眠信条中最不可思议的一应条款的笃信皈依者。他那异乎寻常的敏感状态在我们之间所建立的奇妙纽带，使得我们在一切感觉与感知的目的上，彻底合而为一。我能够追随他漫步涉足其全部出神狂喜的流浪旅程，能够如同我自己服用大麻时那般逼真地看见他所看见之物、感知他所感知之事。然而，正如你所言，这并无任何神奇可言。鉴于我昔日对一切此类状态的彻底熟稔，这完全可能发生，且毫无疑问部分确实是由此促成的。

然而，那种联结纽带绝未止步于此。我喝下了一杯清水，B.感知到这一点的清晰程度，宛若他亲自饮下一水一般。他切身体会到了喉头肌肉的痉挛抽搐——这在大麻状态下的饮水过程中向来如影随形——其逼真程度，以至于他真真切切以为自己在饮水，并苦苦哀求我莫要再给他灌水了。

对于室内的另一人，他向来怀抱强烈的同理共鸣；而此时，这种共鸣更是演化发展至令人惊愕的境地。此人有一个习癖：每逢陷入沉思（brown study）之时，便会极其卖力地揉搓自己的前额，其模样令人观之生痛。骤然间，我听闻B.惊呼道：“啊，鲍勃（Bob），别想了！别再想了！你根本不知道这让我的脑袋有多痛苦！”我的双眼自始至终皆凝视着B.；而他自己的双眸却一次亦未曾睁开过；他又如何能够知晓他的友人正在沉思苦想？我环顾四周，看哪！鲍勃正深陷冥思苦想之中（in medio brown-studio），以其惯有的勤勉卖力地打磨抛光着自己的前额。仅仅凭借他们之间的同理共鸣，B.便洞悉了一切。这或许会招致嘲笑，但若有必要，我极其乐意签署宣誓书作证：在此前整整半个小时里，B.凭借其肉体双眼绝未看见任何事物。在那段时光里，我的双眸未曾有一次移开过他的面庞。

但我仍要继续阐述与我的理论更为直接相关的种种事实。正如我所言，尽管他对自己实际所处的场所毫无丝毫意识，但他依然立足于自己的天国居所同我们侃侃而谈。

在他看来，我们所有人似乎皆一同身处“至高天界（in excelsis）”。在天性上他便是一介充满仁爱与温柔的灵魂；而上方天界的纯净氛围，更是将这一特质淋漓尽致地激发生发了出来。

===Original===

In terms which it would not be modest for any of us to have repeated for ourselves, he expressed his sense of the congenialities which bound us together. But this sense, no less *ethereal* than in the ordinary state, was something far more *visible*.
“I *feel*,” said he, “that we have many mutual ties of fellowship, but, more than that, I *see* them. I know you are feeling kindly to me now, for there are a thousand golden and azure cords which run between us, making a network so exquisite that it is unspeakable delight to look thereon.”
“Are you not fancying it?” said somebody. “Fancying it? how can I fancy that which is immediately before my eyes? Besides that, I realize that it is true; it can not be false; it is a part of each of us delicately prolonged. I see all our characteristics blended in it﻿—oh, it is beautiful﻿—beautiful!”
Here was that inner sense, to which, as most intuitive, we have given by analogy the name of “feeling,” shown to be reciprocal, or, rather, one with sight. But the oneness of the outer senses was also to receive corroboration.
B. looked at us, and as our countenances changed in the course of conversation, that change was embodied to him in tones. “Do you know,” said he, “that all your faces, your forms, have a musical idea? I hear you distinctly, in harp-like notes; each one of you, as you look upon me, has his melody; together your appearance is a harmony. Do you yourselves hear the music which you are?”
While he lay with closed eyes we still talked to him. Now, every sound which we uttered had its being to him, not only in music, but in visible form. Indeed, as he afterward assured me, when in a state to philosophize upon the subject, he read in figures, while we were speaking, every idea as distinctly as from a book. Landscapes, temples, lakes, processions of all kinds of being, passed before him, borne with our voices, and impressed, not with the artificial letter-symbols of our meaning, but with the meaning itself, as in my own case I have expressed it, like an essence made incarnate.
The only sense which was not tested in this experience was that of odor. I have deeply regretted the deficiency ever since, for I am convinced that its oneness with all the others would have been exhibited as clearly as that of the others among themselves. Taste we did try with the fullest result.

===Chinese===

他用我们任何人都难以自夸复述的溢美之词，表达了他对维系我们彼此的相投默契的感知。然而，这种感知丝毫不比日常状态下逊色地显得空灵，甚至展现为远为真切可见的景象。

“我能感知到，”他说道，“我们之间存在着诸多相互交织的手足情谊纽带，但更重要的是，我能亲眼看见它们。我深知你们如今正对我怀抱善意，因为在我们之间穿梭流淌着成千上万道金色与蔚蓝色的细索，织就了一张如此精妙绝伦的罗网，以至于凝视其上便能带来不可言喻的无上欢愉。”

“你难道不是在凭空臆想吗？”有人问道。“凭空臆想？我怎能臆想这赫然伫立在我眼前的景象？不仅如此，我真切意识到它是真实的；它绝不可能是虚假的；它是我们每个人纤细延展的一部分。我看见我们所有人的特质交融融汇于其中——啊，真美啊——太美了！”

在此，那种因其最具直觉性而被我们类比命名为“体感感知”的内在感官，被证明是相互感应的，或者更确切地说，它与视觉合而为一。然而，外部感官的同一性亦同样获得了进一步的印证。

B.注视着我们，随着我们在交谈过程中面容神情的变化，那种变幻在他眼中皆具象化为了音调。“你们可知晓，”他说道，“你们的所有面庞、身形，皆蕴含着一种音乐的理念？我能够清晰地听闻你们，宛若竖琴般的音符；你们当中的每一个人，当你们注视我时，皆拥有其专属的旋律；你们整体的容貌汇聚在一起便是一出和谐的交响。你们自己能听闻到你们所化身而成的音乐吗？”

正当他合上双眸横卧之时，我们依然同他交谈。此时，我们所发出的每一个声响，在他心目中不仅具有音乐般的存在，而且具备具象的形态。事实上，正如他日后在足以针对该课题展开哲学思考的状态下向我保证的那样：在我们说话之际，他能在一幅幅具象图景中研读每一个观念，其清晰程度宛若翻阅一本典籍。山川风景、神殿庙宇、湖泊沼泽、各类生灵的仪仗行列，皆伴随着我们的嗓音在他眼前鱼贯而过，且印刻下来的绝非表达我们含义的人工文字符号，而是含义本身——正如我在此前自身案例中所表达的那样，宛若一种化作肉身的本质。

在这场体验之中，唯独未曾接受检验的感官便是嗅觉。自那以后我便一直对此深感遗憾，因为我坚信，它与其余一切感官的同一性，本会展现得如同其余感官相互之间的同一性那样清晰昭然。而味觉我们确实进行了检验，且斩获了最充分圆满的结果。

===Original===

After much persuasion (for it seemed a degradation of his celestial nature), we prevailed upon him to eat a small piece of an apple. I took a piece of it myself, and if I, who was in heaven, could eat, he might also. Its taste he expressed as giving him likewise the idea of a tone. It was winter, and not a flower of any fragrance was within reach; but I know from my former experience, as well as the fullness of his own in every other respect, that he would have emblematized it in music immediately.
I would that every man whose eye is met by this recital, instead of reading it from my pen, and saying as coldly as is the custom at the present day, “marvelous, but doubtful,” with a shake of the head, could have sat as I did by that sofa, and have learned the truth of this strange theory by an eyewitness as delightful as it was convincing. In not one single lineament of this case have I poetized; indeed, I feel deeply my most signal failure to satisfy my own ideal of what I there saw and felt. I am not aware of any recompense which would tempt me, if I could, to blot out the memory of that most exquisite lesson which I learned at the side of B.
Yet it may be said, “Your own experience had probably been pretty well known to him already, and these perceptions of his were but re-embodiments of things he had heard from you.” I assure you, my dear reader, that of my own experience upon the subject of this unity of sense I had not said a word to him, not even to any person in the place where he lived. His views, from this fact, were perfectly spontaneous, as, indeed, anyone present could have seen from the manner of their natural and irrepressible outflowing in his words. The only possible explanation of such perceptions, occurring as they have in several other cases besides his without any acquaintance with my experience, is that they apprehend real truths, common to all our humanity, and needing but some instrument of intense insight to bring them forth.
Within a few days of this literally clinical lecture upon my theory occurred another case, in some respects almost as singular. Another person, making the hashish experiment for the first time, showed the following strange characteristic in the effect of its influence.

===Chinese===

在一番苦苦劝说之后（因为这在他看来似乎是对其属天神圣天性的玷污降格），我们终于说服他品尝了一小块苹果。我自己亦取了一块，倘若身处天国的我尚能进食，他亦理应能够食用。关于苹果的味道，他表示同样赋予了他一种音调的理念。彼时正值寒冬，触手可及之处毫无半朵带有芳香的花卉；然而无论依据我昔日的体验，还是依据他在其余各个方面所展现出的充沛感知，我皆深知他定会即刻将其用音乐予以象征化呈现。

但愿每一位目睹这篇叙述的人，皆不仅是从我的笔端阅读它，并依照当今世俗的习癖一边摇着头一边冷冷说道“虽奇妙却令人生疑”，而是能够如同我当年那样倚坐在那张沙发身旁，借由一场既令人心旷神怡又令人心悦诚服的亲眼见证，洞悉这一离奇理论的真实不虚。在此案的任何一处细微线索之中，我皆未施加半点诗意夸张；事实上，我深切感到自己极其显著地未能达成自身对当年所见所感的崇高理想。倘若可能，我不认为人世间有任何酬劳能够诱使我抹去在B.身旁所领受的那堂最精妙绝伦的教诲记忆。

然而人们或许会说：“他大概早就对你自身的经历烂熟于心，而他的这些感知无非是对他从你那里听闻之物的重新具象化罢了。”亲爱的读者，我向你担保：关于我自身在感官同一性这一课题上的经历，我未曾向他透露过半个字，甚至未曾向他所居住市镇的任何人提起过。立足于这一事实，他的诸多见解纯属全然自发的产物，一如当时在场的任何人皆能从其言辞中自然流淌且无可遏制的涌动方式所看清的那样。对此类感知（除了他之外亦曾出现在对其余数人的案例中，且他们皆对我昔日的经历毫不知情）唯一可行的解释，便是它们捕捉到了切切实实属于全人类共通的客观真理，仅仅需要某种激发强烈洞察力的工具将其昭示引出罢了。

在关于我这项理论的这场名副其实的“临床示范课”过去数日之后，又发生了一桩在某些方面几乎同样奇绝的案例。另一名初次施行大麻实验之人，在其药力影响的效应中展现出了如下奇异特质。

===Original===

Though as perfectly conscious as in his natural state, and capable of apprehending all outer realities without hallucination, he still perceived every word which was spoken to him in the form of some visible symbol which most exquisitely embodied it. For hours every sound had its color and its form to him as truly as scenery could have them.
The fact, never witnessed by me before, of a mind in that state being able to give its phenomena to another and philosophize about them calmly, afforded me the means of a most clear investigation. I found that his case was exactly analogous to those of B. and myself; for, like us, he recognized in distinct inner types every possible sensation, our words making a visible emblematic procession before his eyes, and every perception, of whatever sense, becoming tangible to him as form, and audible as music.
There is something more than the mere fascinating activity of speculation in knowing such things as these. The excellency of their office consists in acquainting us with the fact that in our minds we possess a far greater wealth than we have ever conceived. Such a discovery may do much for us in every way, making material ends seem less valuable to us as ultimate aims, and encouraging us to live well for the sake of a spirit which possesses fathomless capacities for happiness no less than knowledge.
There is a condition in which the soul may exist, which is possible (and when we have proved anything possible for a soul, we have, at the same time, proved it probable), in which every object of our perception shall infuse into us all the delight of whose modifications now but one alone trickles in parsimoniously through a single sense. With a more ethereal organization, the necessity for dividing our perception into the five or six modes now known may utterly pass away, and the full harmony of all qualities capable of teaching or delighting us may flow in at once to ravish the soul.
In the cases which I have mentioned, hashish had nearly perfected this etherealization already. Yet hashish must be forborne; we have no right to succeed to the inheritance till we come of age. In our longing for that spiritual majority which is to invest us with our title, we may stay ourselves on prophecies as well as patience.

===Chinese===

尽管他与身处自然状态时一样意识清醒健全，且有能力在毫无幻觉侵扰的情况下捕捉一切外部现实，但他依然将对他所说的每一个字词，感知为某种极其精妙具象化该词的可见符号形态。在长达数小时的时间里，每一个声音对他而言皆具有其特有的色彩与形态，其真切程度正如自然山川风景具备形色一般无二。

一颗身处此种状态的心智能够将其内在现象呈现给旁人并冷静平心静气地展开哲学思辨——这一我此前从未亲眼见证过的事实——为我提供了一场极其明晰的调查探究契机。我发觉他的案例同B.与我自身的经历严丝合缝；因为同我们一样，他亦在清晰的内在类型中辨识出了每一种可能的感觉，我们的言辞在他眼前化作了一场清晰可见的象征行列，而属于任何感官的每一种感知，对他而言皆化作了可触及的形态，化作了可倾听的音乐。

通晓此类事物，其意义远不止于思辨本身的迷人魅力。其功用的崇高卓绝之处，在于让我们知晓这样一个事实：在我们自己的心智深处，蕴藏着远超我们此前所构想的丰厚财富。此类发现在方方面面皆能给我们带来莫大的裨益，使物质目的作为终极追求在我们眼中显得不再那般尊贵，并鼓舞我们为了一个不仅具备深不可测的求知潜能、更具备无尽幸福潜能的灵魂而好好活着。

灵魂能够栖居存在于这样一种可能达致的境况之中（当我们证明了某种状态对灵魂而言是可能的，我们便同时证明了其具有极大的概率）：届时，我们感知所及的每一个客体，皆将向我们倾注其所能激发的全部欢愉，而当下的我们却唯有借助单一感官孤零零、吝啬地汲取其中的细微修饰碎片。借由一种更为轻盈空灵的机体构筑，将我们的感知割裂拆分为如今已知的五种或六种模态的必然性或将彻底烟消云散，而一切能够教化或愉悦我们的属性的完备和谐交响，皆将一涌而入，令灵魂沉醉痴迷。

在我所提及的诸多案例之中，大麻几乎已然将这种轻盈空灵化推向了登峰造极之境。然而我们必须克制戒绝大麻；在尚未达致法定成年年龄之前，我们毫无权利继承这份基业。在我们对那赋予我们合法名分的属灵成年的渴盼之中，我们既可凭借预言自持，亦可凭借耐心坚守。

===Original===

Perchance we may listen to some such prophecy as this: There is a land, oh dreamer, on which the sun rises in music, and his rays are heard sounding symphony to the greeting of Memnon. The ever-shifting tints of cloudland forever rise into brightness and anthems, and fall back again to softness and lullabies. The fingers of the harper paint exquisite green fields with the pencil of a tone, and the child that sings by his side fills the soul with Claude Lorraine sunsets. The clasp of a brother’s hand returning from over sea is felt in a rosy heaven, or the light of one more star and a thrill of glad-hearted song. The meaning of the brotherhood between wine and carols is known by a strain of music from the terraces of Rhine and the vineyards of Xeres, bathing the lips of the poet in added melody. With the fall of the sun upon empurpled cloud-banks of the west, the fragrance of the flowers floats to him in a hymn of good night, and the wind from his portals rings a curfew upon lily and rose. Land twice blessed, where all things are manifold in their melodies, harmonious in difference!
Thus did I prophesy to myself, as, according to my wont, with closed eyes I sat listening to a sonata of Beethoven. Within me the prophecy was even now half fulfilled, for I was dreaming in a land of palaces builded of tones, a country whose rivers ebbed and flowed with the modulations of the outer music.
Are we persuaded of these things that we may be deceived? is our hope in vain? There is nothing too beautiful to believe of the soul. If its visions seem falsified by matter, it is only because they are above matter; because in prophetic gazings it mirrors a higher, a more ethereal incarnation of the Creative Spirit than yet communes with it through the passages of the fleshly sense.

===Chinese===

或许我们能够倾听这般预言的低语：幻梦者啊，存在着这样一片乐土，彼处的旭日是在音乐中破晓升起的，其万道霞光化作了迎候门农（Memnon）巨像的交响轰鸣。云海幻境那变幻莫测的斑斓色彩，永恒升腾为明灿光华与激昂赞歌，又缓缓沉降回柔和静谧与安眠摇篮曲。竖琴手的十指以音调为画笔，勾勒出秀美绝伦的碧绿田园；而身旁引吭高歌的孩童，则让灵魂浸润在克劳德·洛兰（Claude Lorraine）笔下的落日余晖之中。从重洋彼岸归来的手足相握之手，在一片玫瑰色的天国中为人所感知，或化作又一颗星辰的温润光华与一曲欢欣雀跃的激荡歌谣。美酒与欢歌之间手足相连的深意，借由莱茵河畔层层台地与赫雷斯（Xeres）葡萄园所传来的旋律乐章为人所洞悉，给诗人的唇齿洗礼上一抹额外的音韵。随着落日西沉于西方泛着紫红的层叠云海，花卉的芬芳在晚安赞美诗中向他飘然而至，而从其门廊吹拂而来的晚风，在百合与玫瑰的花瓣上敲响了安歇的黄昏宵禁钟声。得蒙双重恩眷的福地啊，在彼处，万物的旋律纷繁多样，于殊异之中绽放出神圣的和谐！

正如我依着惯常习癖闭目静听贝多芬（Beethoven）的奏鸣曲那样，我对自己发出了这般预言。在我心头，这一预言甚至在当下便已达成了大半，因为我正漫步徜徉在一片由音调构筑的宫殿乐土之中，在这片国度里，江河川流随着外部音乐的转调起伏而潮涨潮落。

我们笃信这些事物，难道仅仅是为了遭受欺瞒吗？我们的期盼难道终归虚妄？关于灵魂，绝无任何事物美丽到令人不敢置信的境地。倘若它的幻象在物质面前看似被伪造驳斥，仅仅是因为它们凌驾于物质之上；因为在预言性的凝视之中，它映照出的是造物之灵（Creative Spirit）一种更为崇高、更为轻盈空灵的化身，远非那仍借由肉体肉感孔道同其交融神会的存在所能比拟。

## Ideal Men and Their Stimulants / 理想之人与其兴奋剂
===Original===

Of all the infinitely plastic shapes of language, perhaps the most Protean is that word “The World.” Monosyllable as it is, it bears upon its back a load of incongruous meanings immense enough to have crushed into nothingness a dozen of the statelier sesquipedalia, which do not draw the marrow of their stubborn reality from so stanch a Saxon genealogy, nor plant their feet so firmly upon the usages of our hard, everyday life. In that word we see the triumph of Saxonism, for it is astonishing how any word which means so many things has not finally come to mean nothing definite at all, a “*vox et praeterea nihil.*” While it has held its place, many other words have been banished from the common parlance of men, or are allowed only when they can be explained by their context, or when vagueness itself is an especial desideratum.
Not to multiply instances, let the English “good” and the French “*vertu*” be examples. The first of these who ever thinks of using (we limit its reference to human attributes) when he wishes to express something defined of the character of another? The poets only, for they, indeed, from the picturesque necessities of their art, have preserved its original outlines clear, and give it always its noble, radical force; the good man, with them, is the man of developed heart as distinguished from his clever brother of the developed intellect. But in common conversation, to say that a man is good tells about as much of him as to ascribe to him the possession of a nose. He may be, for all we know, a sour Pharisee, held righteous in proportion to the number of things which he considers sins, an easy soul who does or does not pay his little bills, a kindly person of fluent sympathies, or

> “A good-humored dunce with the best of intentions.”

===Chinese===

在语言那千变万化、极具塑性的诸般形态之中，或许最为诡谲善变的莫过于“世界（The World）”一词了。纵然它不过是个单音节词，肩头却背负着如此庞杂不伦的浩瀚意涵，其沉重程度足以将一打更为宏伟的长篇大词碾压成齑粉——而那些冗词长句既未曾从坚挺纯正的撒克逊词源谱系中汲取其坚韧现实的骨髓，亦未曾如此坚实地扎根于我们冷酷严苛的日常生活习俗之上。在这一词汇之中，我们目睹了撒克逊语风格的伟绩；因为令人惊叹的是：一个涵括如此繁多意蕴的词汇，竟终究未曾沦为某种毫无确凿意旨之物，未曾沦为所谓的“空洞回响（vox et praeterea nihil）”。当它稳稳维系其地位之际，诸多其他词汇已被逐出世人的通用口语，或是唯有在能够凭借上下文加以阐释之时、亦或当含混本身成为某种特殊渴求之物时，方才获准出场。

姑且无须枚举无数例证，且以英语中的“好（good）”与法语中的“德性（vertu）”为例。在人们企图确凿表达他人品格的某种特质之时，谁曾念及去使用前一个词（我们将其指涉局限于人类属性）？唯有诗人们方才如此，因为出于其艺术对生动如画的必然需求，他们确实将该词原初的鲜明轮廓保留了下来，且始终赋予其崇高而根本的力量；在他们笔下，“好人”乃是拥有充沛心智情感之人，以区别于其拥有过人智力的聪慧兄弟。然而在寻常攀谈之中，声称一个人“好”，对其本质的揭示程度大概等同于描述他“长着一只鼻子”。据我们所知，他或许是一介尖酸刻薄的法利赛人，其公义程度仅仅取决于被他视作罪衍的事物数量；或许是一介凡事随和、或付或欠其琐碎账单的糊涂虫；或许是一介满怀流转同情之心的仁善之士；亦或许是——

> “一介怀抱至善好意、性情温厚的蠢材。”

===Original===

To the word “good,” a man with the bump of Causality prominent must always reply “How?” and then come a host of particularizations on the other side to round out the idea.
In like manner has “*vertu*” passed utterly out of the universal sparkling Gallic mouth, not only for the reason that the idea which it embodies exists in a somewhat misty (as well as musty) state in the national brain, but because, very likely as a necessary outflow from this fact, it has been dissolved under the pressure of its numerous meanings into free vapor. The person who cultivates “*la vertu*” may be conceived of as a man who prefers reading the *Constitutionnel* of an evening to his wife, in slippers, to the society of Lorettes at the *Bal au Masque*; or again, for aught that we know, he may this moment be looking for medallions of Claudius in green spectacles and Pompeii.
But “the world,” word as truly as thing, has held its course. We do not confound “the man of the world” with Humboldt, who has traveled all over it, nor “the ways of the world” with relations to its tumbling around from day to night, and from peri- into ap-helion. We understand every man in the speciality of meaning which he chooses to stamp upon the word, and pass on without further questioning.
With the Geographer it means﻿—no matter, we know what it means, not having in early youth blasphemed to no purpose the American idea of universal enlightenment over an Atlas. With the Ethnologist it is an affiliation of human manners; with the Philologist, a brotherhood of tongues. The man of society says “the world,” and straightway it paints to him, if transatlantic, a vision of Almacks and the Clubs; if cisatlantic, a prodigality of entreés on the Avenue. A circle of spinsters whisper the mystic symbol over souchong, and lo! at uncontaminating distance, a dream of deluded souls dancing into inevitable destruction to a Redowa discoursed from Dodworth’s balefully-fascinating tubes. Yet, by a more catholic appreciation than we give to any other word, in each case we catch the full force of the particular idea. O world, as word alone, truly there is no “transit” to thy “gloria.”
Yet, from the very ease with which it carries its multiplicity of meaning, we are apt to forget how manifold they really are.

===Chinese===

面对“好”这个字眼，一个“因果机能（Causality）”头骨凸起显著之人，必然始终会追问一句“如何个好法？”，随后对方必须抛出一连串的详尽列举，方能将这一观念填补圆满。

同理，“德性（vertu）”一词亦已彻底从高卢人普遍神采飞扬的唇齿间消逝绝迹，这不仅是因为它所承载的观念在该民族的大脑中处于某种略显朦胧（亦显陈腐）的状态，更是因为——作为这一事实极其自然的必然流露——它在自身繁复意涵的重压之下已然融解蒸发为游离的虚无蒸汽。修习“德性（la vertu）”之人，既可被设想为一名夜晚宁愿穿着拖鞋给妻子朗读《宪法报》（*Constitutionnel*）、亦不愿在假面舞会（*Bal au Masque*）上周旋于交际花之列的男子；亦可能在此时此刻，据我们所知，他正戴着绿色护目镜在庞贝古城（Pompeii）搜寻克劳狄一世（Claudius）的浮雕勋章。

然而“世界”一词，无论作为词汇还是作为实体，皆始终我行我素地行进在自己的轨道上。我们绝不会将“世故之人（man of the world）”同踏遍世界各地的洪堡（Humboldt）混为一谈，亦绝不会将“人情世故（ways of the world）”同地球自昼入夜的自转以及由近日点至远日点的公转混为一谈。我们完全能够在他选择烙印在这一词汇之上的特定意涵中理解每一个人，且毫无疑义地任其通行。

在地理学家口中，它的含义乃是——无论如何，我们心知肚明其意，毕竟在少年时代我们并未无休止地对着一张地图集去徒然亵渎美国全民启蒙的理念。在人种学家眼中，它是人类风俗的亲缘谱系；在语文学家笔下，它是诸般语种的手足同盟。社交界的体面人提及“世界”，在他脑海中便会即刻勾勒出一幅图景——倘若身处大西洋彼岸，便是阿尔马克社交界（Almacks）与各大绅士俱乐部；倘若身处大西洋此岸，则是第五大道上铺张奢华的各色名馔头盘。一群待字闺中的老处女就着小种红茶窃窃私语着这句神秘暗语，看哪！在不染纤尘的安全距离之外，浮现出一段虚妄灵魂的幻梦：伴随着多德沃思（Dodworth）那令人着魔的管乐器吹奏出的雷多瓦舞曲（Redowa），舞步正滑向不可逆转的毁灭深渊。然而，借由一种比我们赋予其余任何词汇皆更为宽博包容的鉴赏领悟力，在每一种情境下，我们皆能彻底捕捉到那一特定观念的充沛分量。啊，世界啊，单单作为词汇而言，你的“荣耀”委实永不“衰颓（transit）”。

然而，恰恰源于它承载繁复意涵时所展现出的举重若轻，我们往往极易淡忘那些意涵究竟是何等绚丽多样。

===Original===

We thus lose sight of a truth, than which there is none more actual, that, though we intermarry, walk, talk, and transact business together, we are each of us, this moment, living in a different world.
Even as a mere bald question of the bodily senses, this is beyond a doubt. A is a near sighted man, and has a very defective power of discriminating colors. Like several men whom I know, he may be utterly unable to distinguish the strawberry from its leaf, or, like certain others, to discriminate between the fly on his spectacles and the eagle in the firmament. B, on the other hand, sees ships in the offing before the signalman has got the focus, and pronounces dogmatically, at a glance, between two shades of blue which do not differ from each other by a tenth. A and B live in two absolutely different sight-worlds.
Again, C perceives no difference between sounds or harmonies. He is, let us say, a celebrated divine whom I have the honor to know. Some years ago, when “Oh Susannah” was triumphantly ushering in the Ethiopian school of composition to popular favor, a roguish daughter of the gentleman happened to be playing that exquisite air upon the piano, and (much as I regret to state it) upon a Sunday morning also. Her father, struck with the novel beauty of the music, although he had heard it fifty times before, asked what it was, and was answered, with a sense of security which based itself upon his peculiar auricular failing, that it was “Greenland’s icy mountains.”
In the evening all the family were assembled in the parlor, and Dr. —﻿—﻿— asked the fair rogue to play the piece that had so much pleased him in the morning. Of course, by the family, “Oh Susannah” was reckoned among secular melodies, and, to speak popularly, would not “go down.” Without a moment’s hesitation, Miss —﻿—﻿— awoke the instrument to “Greenland,” and the doctor was as perfectly satisfied as he had been ten hours before.
Such a one we will say is C. D, on the contrary, recognizes fifteen gradations between F and G of the natural scale, and whistles every air in Trovatore on his way home from its first performance at the Academy. If an itinerant miller of music, “knowing the wally of peace and quietness,” refuses to move under a shilling, he makes over the additional sixpence and thinks it clear gain.
The sound-worlds of C and D are as truly twain as Mars and Jupiter.

===Chinese===

我们因而忽视了一项比其余一切现实皆更为切近的真理：那便是，纵然我们彼此通婚、同行、攀谈并共谋生计，然而在此时此刻，我们当中的每一个人，皆生活在截然不同的世界之中。

即便单纯作为肉体感官的直白议题而言，这一点亦确凿无疑。A是一介近视之人，在辨析色彩上存在着极大的缺陷。正如我所熟识的数人一样，他可能完全无法将草莓同其叶片区分开来；或者正如另一些人那样，根本无法界分落在自己眼镜片上的苍蝇与翱翔在穹苍之上的雄鹰。反观B，早在信号员尚未调好焦距之前便已窥见了远海天际线上的船舶，且一眼便能武断地在两种差异不足十分之一的蓝色色调之间明断秋毫。A与B生活在两座绝对迥异的“视觉世界”之中。

再者，C对声音或和声的差异毫无感知。姑且设想他是我有幸结识的一位著名神学家吧。若干年前，正当《噢，苏珊娜》（Oh Susannah）凯歌高奏地将埃塞俄比亚黑人学派（Ethiopian school）的曲风引入大众流行狂潮之时，这位先生淘气顽皮的女儿恰好在钢琴上弹奏起这首优美动听的曲调——且（尽管我极不情愿提及）恰恰发生在一个主日的清晨。她的父亲被这首乐曲新颖的美感所打动，尽管他此前已听过不下五十遍，却依然询问这是何曲目；而女儿立足于其父独特的听觉缺陷所赋予的安全感，回答道那是《格陵兰的冰山》（Greenland’s icy mountains）。

入夜时分，全家人聚集在客厅之中，那位博士便请求那位美丽的淘气姑娘弹奏清晨令他如此心旷神怡的那首曲子。当然，在这家人眼中，《噢，苏珊娜》被视作世俗靡靡之音，套用俗话说便是“登不得大雅之堂”。那位小姐毫不迟疑，当即在琴键上奏响了《格陵兰》，而博士的心满意足之情，丝毫不亚于十个小时之前。

我们便说此类人乃是C。反之，D却能在自然音阶的F音与G音之间辨识出十五种微细层级，且在音乐学院首场演出散场归家的途中吹响《游吟诗人》（Trovatore）的每一段旋律。倘若某个流浪的风琴演奏艺人“深谙安宁清静的身价”，非要拿上一先令才肯挪步，他便会毫不犹豫多掏出六便士，且自以为占了大便宜。

C与D所居处的声音世界，其判若两极的程度丝毫不亚于火星之于木星。

===Original===

But I will not consume the letters of the alphabet in any further analysis of a statement so apparent to the slightest thought. Just briefly, in their relations to the remaining senses, let me set the opposite types apart.
In Touch, on one side stands the artisan who, with his finger, can measure that convexity in a lens which few men could determine by the eye; on the other, the person who scarcely, by his hand, discovers any inequality in a board, provided it be planed. In connection with the former, I might mention such a case as that of Giovanni Gonelli, of Volterra, who, in the seventeenth century, gave to the world the spectacle of a man entirely blind, yet a most accurate sculptor, not alone of his own ideals, but of faces which he only knew by passing his hand over them. Among the likenesses which he left were those, both faithful and beautiful, of Cosmo di Medici, Pope Urban VIII, and the first Charles of England. Yet, though I have refrained, on account of the exceptional nature of this case, I might well adduce other instances of blind dexterity and delicacy of touch far from exceptional.
Again, in Smell, there are innumerable grades between the person in whom it is an absolute lack and the one to whom, our world being unfortunately not a universal spice-grove, it is a source of constant misery. At this moment I am writing but a few feet from a lady who, a day or two ago, assured me that if, by any operation, however painful, she could eradicate her sense of smell without danger, she would willingly submit to it, even though it cost her those rose and jasmine odors in which she delights with more intensity than practical people do in a good dinner.
In Taste we may shade off humanity between the two extremes of an Apicius, desolé on account of the one quarter grain of ambergris more than the receipt in his soup of flamingos’ tongues, and the Scotchman who, outside of his herring and his bannocks, is at sea upon all delicate questions of gustative interest.
In Feeling, as defined in the preceding note, the sense, to speak in general terms, of pains and pleasures not comprehended by other organs, the grades are almost innumerable.
There is a case on record of a lady so exquisitely constituted in this respect that the recital of another’s pain in any particular member immediately made her feel it acutely in her own.

===Chinese===

但我不再耗费字母表的其余字母去进一步剖析一项只需稍加思索便昭然若揭的陈述了。且容我就其余感官的范畴，简明扼要地将两极对立的典型分门别类加以列举。

在触觉（Touch）之中，一端伫立着能用手指测定透镜凸度而唯有极少数人能用肉眼看出的匠人；另一端则伫立着只要木板经过刨平、用双手几乎摸不出任何凹凸不平之人。关于前者，我愿提及诸如沃尔泰拉（Volterra）的乔瓦尼·戈内利（Giovanni Gonelli）这样的案例：在17世纪，他向世人展现了一幅双目完全失明、却能成为最精确雕塑家的奇观——不仅雕刻自己的理想构想，更雕刻那些他仅仅用手抚摸面庞便了然于胸的面孔。在他留下的塑像之中，包括了对科西莫·德·美第奇（Cosmo di Medici）、教皇乌尔班八世（Pope Urban VIII）以及英格兰查理一世（Charles of England）既逼真传神又优美绝伦的肖像。然而，尽管鉴于该案例的特殊性我未多加赘述，我完全可以引述盲人敏捷身手与细腻触觉的其余例证，而那些例证绝非罕见特例。

再者，在嗅觉（Smell）之中，在彻底匮乏此项感官之人与因我们的世界不幸并非处处皆为香料丛林而使其沦为无休止痛苦之源的人之间，存在着无数细微层级。在此时此刻，我正坐在距离一位女士仅仅数英尺的地方写作，而她在一两天前曾向我保证：倘若能通过任何手术（无论多么痛苦）毫无危险地彻底根除其嗅觉，她亦甘愿欣然受之，纵使这会让她丧失对玫瑰与茉莉芳香的感知——而她对这些香气的沉醉狂热程度，远甚于务实之人对一顿美餐的执念。

在味觉（Taste）之中，我们亦能将人类划分为两极光谱：一端是阿皮基乌斯（Apicius）之流，仅仅因其红鹤舌汤中比食谱多放了四分之一格令的龙涎香便悲痛欲绝；另一端则是苏格兰人，除了鲱鱼与燕麦饼之外，在一切涉及美味关切的幽微问题上皆茫然不知所措。

在体感知觉（Feeling）之中——如前章注释中所界定的，笼统而言即其余器官未能涵盖的痛感与快感感知——其层级更是浩瀚不可胜数。

曾有一项见诸记载的案例：一位女士在此方面的情感构造极其精微敏锐，以至于聆听旁人诉说某个特定肢体的痛苦，便会即刻令她自己的相同部位感到撕心裂肺般的剧痛。

===Original===

I might offset to this the instance of a person who avowed to me that the extraction of the largest molar give her as little suffering as the scratch of a pin, and she dreaded no possible operation to such an extent as to care to use an anesthetic. Since she was at all times characteristically matter of fact, and never adorned the blank reality of her ideas with fiction, I had no reason to doubt that she rigorously meant what she said.
Here, then, we see both nature and cultivation making infinite variety in individual acuteness and range of all the senses. In the words of the great Chadband, “What does this teach us?”
There is, no doubt, an objective world, a something external to our perception, and outside of our originating energy, which produces the effects by us called collectively “the world.” Yet, in order to become a thing perceived, that something must undergo a modification by our organs, which, after all, makes us as truly actors in the being of the world, for all purposes of perception, as if we had helped to create it. Accordingly as the senses vary, so also will the world vary, becoming all things to all men; and literally the same thing to no two men. So, not metaphorically at all, but in the most restricted sense, every human being of us has his own world which no other man has any conception of, and this, too, with all our senses wide open, and, if you please, looking in the same direction. Only upon abstract mathematical truths, or on the forum of axioms, do we ever come exactly together, and do business with each other by the same balances. Once off of this common ground, and, though we talk about things the same in words, we mean something which we see and feel very differently. The husband does not know exactly how his voice sounds to his wife, nor the wife whether to her husband her face looks precisely as it does to herself in the glass. All that they can be tolerably sure of in their intercourse with one another is that they hit the same general and necessary facts.
But if in the mere bodily senses we find such different worlds, how much more is it the case in our spiritual organism. From the characteristic of this variation we utterly exempt that faculty of direct insight which beholds truths that are necessary and therefore universal.

===Chinese===

我完全可以举出另一位人士的案例与之形成对照：她曾向我坦言，拔除一颗最大的臼齿给她带来的痛苦，同被大头针划破皮肤毫无二致，且世间绝无任何手术能令她畏惧到甘愿使用麻醉剂的地步。鉴于她在任何时候皆是极具求真务实品格之人，且从未用虚构去粉饰其观念的纯粹现实，我毫无理由怀疑她并非字字当真。

至此，我们便目睹了天然禀赋与后天培育如何在所有感官的个体敏锐度与范畴之上铸就了无限的多样性。套用伟大的查德班德（Chadband）的话来说：“这昭示了我们什么呢？”

毫无疑问，存在着一个客观世界，某种独立外在于我们感知、且外在于我们原初生发能量的实体，它制造了被我们统称为“世界”的种种效应。然而，欲成为一件被感知之物，该实体必须经受我们器官的修饰调节——归根结底，就感知的一切目的而言，这使得我们在这座世界的存在之中扮演着真真切切的行动者角色，正如我们曾参与构筑创造了它一样。随着感官的千差万别，世界亦必然随之千变万化，对所有人显现为不同之物；且在字面意义上，对任意两人而言皆绝非完全同一之物。因此，绝非借助任何比喻修辞，而是在最严格限定的意义上，我们之中的每一个凡人皆拥有属于他自己、且任何其余之人皆毫无概念的专属世界——且这还是在我们将所有感官彻底敞开、哪怕面朝同一方向凝视的前提之下。唯独在抽象数学真理之上，或在公理的讲坛之中，我们方能真正汇聚一堂，并依凭完全相同的天平彼此权衡共事。一旦脱离了这块共通的立足基石，纵然我们在字面上谈论着完全相同的事物，我们所意指的却是我们所见所感截然不同之物。丈夫绝无法确切知晓自己的嗓音在妻子耳畔究竟听起来如何，妻子亦无法知晓自己的面容在丈夫眼中是否恰如她在镜中所端详到的自身模样。在彼此的交融往来之中，他们所能差强人意确信的，无非是他们触碰到了完全相同的大体且必然的事实。

然而，倘若单单在肉体感官之中我们便能发掘出如此迥然不同的世界，那么在我们的精神机能之中又该是何等景象啊！我们必须将直接洞察的机能从这种差异性的特质中彻底豁免出来——该机能所洞悉的乃是必然因而亦是普世的永恒真理。

===Original===

This, which may be called the Intuition of Truth, is not only the same in its perceptions, but pretty nearly equal in its scope among all men. None but idiots, of whatever land or tribe, could fail to see that a straight line is the shortest distance between two points, and in the field of ideas to which that belongs there is at present a small harvest of similar facts, and none but men preternaturally exalted have reaped any more from richer heights.
Leave this plane, and we are all irreconcilable again. That which is one man’s darling goal in life is the loathing or hatred of his neighbor. We are astonished at each other’s attachments; and while we forget the old “de gustibus” aphorism, we forget also another thing whose remembrance would be much more apt to keep us calm than any dogmatic assertion of a fact without its reasons, like that of the proverb. My dear sir, the object of your friend’s attachment you do not perceive in the slightest. With the index of a word out of your common dictionary he points in a certain direction. You look, and see something which does not please you. Do not growl for that fact; if he had your spiritual eyes, he would see something that did not please him; had you his, you would see an object as lovable as he himself sees.
The importance of a proposed measure, the value of a certain end to be secured, are utterly different with different individual judgments. The majority which wins the day must not be understood as a body of men who all think alike. Each individual mind composing it sees the question in a light varying by inexpressible shades from that which illuminates each of his colleagues. The majority is nothing more nor less than a collection of minds who, seeing one proposition in certain connections, varying in each case, think they all understand it as the same, and consent to let go their minor views with relation to it for the sake of carrying through that which on the whole they believe to be the best, though for very different ends.
There are philosophers who seriously lament over this infinite variance of perceptions, judgments, and feelings, as if it were the grand obstacle in the track of human perfection.
Deferentially, though candidly, I acknowledge that I think this a mistake.

===Chinese===

这一或可被称为“真理直觉（Intuition of Truth）”的机能，不仅在其感知上高度同一，且在全人类之中的涵盖范畴亦大体相等。无论来自哪个国度或部族，除了白痴痴呆之人外，绝无人会看不出两点之间直线最短；且在该原则所归属的观念领域之中，如今已然收获了少许类似的公理事实，而除了那些天赋异禀、精神超常拔高之人外，无人能从更为丰饶的高处斩获更多。

一旦脱离这一层面，我们所有人便立刻重新沦为不可调和的对立面。被某人视作毕生孜孜以求的心爱目标，在邻人眼中却是厌恶或仇视的对象。我们对彼此的钟爱依恋感到惊愕不已；而当我们忘却那句“各有所好（de gustibus）”的古老格言之时，我们亦忘却了另一桩事物——铭记此事，远比铭记谚语那种毫无缘由的武断事实断言更能让我们平心静气。我亲爱的先生啊，对于令尊友钟爱依恋的客体，你实则毫无丝毫感知。他不过是借由你们共通词典中的某个词汇索引指向了某个特定方向。你放眼望去，看见了某种令你不悦之物。切莫为此大动肝火；倘若他拥有你的精神双眸，他亦会看见令他不快之物；而倘若你拥有他的眼睛，你便会看见一个如他亲眼所见那般可爱动人的客体。
