一项拟议举措的重要性，某个旨在达成的目标的价值，在不同的个体判断中完全大相径庭。在博弈中胜出的多数派，绝不可被理解为一群思想步调一致之人。构成多数派的每一个独立心智，在审视该问题时所依凭的视角，皆同照亮其同僚的视角存在着难以言表的微妙偏差。多数派无非是一群心智的汇聚：他们在各自不同的特定关联中审视同一命题，自以为所有人都对其达成了相同的理解，并愿意搁置自身与该命题相关的细枝末节分歧，以便推行他们在总体上认为最上乘的举措——纵然各自所抱持的目的截然不同。

有些哲学家严肃哀叹这种感知、判断与情感上的无尽差异，宛若这是阻碍人类走向完善的崇山峻岭。

怀着敬意与坦诚，我必须承认：我认为这纯属一种误解。

===Original===

Indeed, the problem of our humanity standing as it does thus﻿—Given our present nature, and the necessity arising out of it that investigation should be the instrument of acquiring wisdom, what is the best possible contrivance for furthering the operation? I would reply, this very state of omnipresent variance. Supposing that suddenly, and just at the point in all science which we have now reached, the law of mind should change, and, a great average being struck, we should all, not to make an extreme case by saying throughout the world, but merely over its civilized area, henceforward see everything precisely alike, and precisely alike be affected by everything which we saw﻿—it seems to me that a worse calamity could not happen to mankind. The wheels of our spiritual progress now roll somewhat erratically, it is true, as the impulse of the hosts who urge on the chariot is stronger now on this side, now on that, but the resultant of all the forces is a rapid and a forward motion. The check which would ensue to that progress from the coming in of an entire uniformity would be sufficient to retard for centuries the millennium of mind. True, all would push in one direction, but the grand nisus, the energy of ambition, would be lost.
In the contests﻿—yes, even in the quarrels of opinion, we have a guarantee for the development of truth. Fertility is not the characteristic of unbroken plains; they become the torrid desert or the icy steppe; but it bestows itself upon a grouping of entire opposites; the peaks catch the clouds, and with them water the valleys. As the collision of flint and steel gives fire, so from the crashing together of many adverse views comes out Truth, the bright, the beautiful, the eternal. Let us thank God that human thought and human feeling are not one vast stagnant lake, but a sea whose ever-struggling and colliding waves keep their mass pure, and cleanse the intellectual atmosphere.
Our great need is not a reducing to uniformity, but a purging from all acrimony in our contests, the infusion of a willingness to permit and a readiness to appreciate all those differences of form, which, in every one of our neighbors, opinion must necessarily take.

===Chinese===

事实上，关于我们人性的课题既然如此呈现——鉴于我们当下的天性，以及由此生发出的必须将探索调查视作斩获智慧工具的必然性，究竟何种构想才是推动这一运作的最优路径？我会回答：正是这种无处不在的纷繁差异状态。假定骤然之间，恰恰在我们当今所有科学所达致的关节点上，心智的法则发生了剧变，且抹平出一个庞大的平均水准，使得我们所有人——姑且无须推向全天下的极端，仅仅在其文明开化之地——从此对一切事物的看法皆绝对同一，且对所见的一切事物皆受到完全相同的感染触动——在我看来，世间绝无任何比这更悲惨的灾祸降临于人类了。诚然，我们精神前进的巨轮如今运转得略显摇摆不定，因为驱动战车的众人冲劲时而偏向这一侧、时而偏向那一侧，然而一切合力的合力，却是一场迅捷而坚定的大步前行。全面一致性的降临对这一演进所造成的阻遏，足以将心智千禧年的到来拖延数个世纪之久。诚然，所有人皆会朝着同一方向推搡，然而那股宏阔的奋进意图（nisus）与雄心壮志的澎湃能量，却将彻底荡然无存。

在观点的交锋博弈——乃至在观点的争吵龃龉之中，我们获得了真理生发演进的坚实保障。肥沃丰茂绝非一马平川的原野所独有的特质；平原终将沦为焦灼的荒漠或苦寒的荒原冻土；而丰饶往往垂青于截然对立事物的群聚交汇：奇峰捕获云海，并借此浇灌润泽山谷。正如燧石同精钢的撞击能激发出星火一样，正是从诸多针锋相对观点的激烈碰撞之中，迸发出了真理——那明澈、优美、永恒的真理。且让我们感谢上帝：人类的思想与人类的情感绝非一汪浩瀚死寂的滞水死湖，而是一座波澜壮阔的大海，其永恒搏击激荡的浪潮维系着自身水体的纯净，并荡涤着整个智识的天空氛围。

我们最迫切的需求绝非削足适履归于划一，而是涤荡除尽我们在博弈交锋中的一切尖酸刻薄，注入一种宽容允许并敏于鉴赏一切形态差异的开阔胸襟——而那些形态差异，正是观点在我们的每一个邻人身上所必然呈现的模样。

===Original===

We have not all to bow down at the same shrine, but to respect those of all other men while we worship at our own; to put down the iconoclastic hammer, though not pretending to burn incense before one great average god of sentiment.
This tolerance is yet to be learned, for it is not a remarkably flourishing virtue even of the nineteenth century. Our great advance at this day has been made in the direction of refining our intolerance. For the stake and the dungeon have been substituted the taunt and the sneer, an invective which burns more lingeringly than the former, and a neglect which surpasses the latter in its fatal chill. We have yet to open our ears to the Past, which, up to our present summit of enlightenment and vision, is calling forever, in sad and earnest litany, “By Smithfield and the Lollards’ Tower, by the poverty of confiscation and the weariness of banishment, by the blood of Savonarola, and Galileo shut up in prison from his stars, be merciful﻿—be tolerant!”
There is one excellent result of this great multiplicity of worlds which we seldom value as we ought. Who that of a morning walks up Broadway, in one of the two currents of that hurrying life, does not wonder that all the thousands who are rushing on, each for the sustenance or gratification of self, do not oftener jostle through that very selfishness, that the crowds do not interpenetrate each other with more friction? As a fact, we see them tolerably calm, obliging, and self-continent. As a problem, supposing it given to a philosopher who calculated only upon the data of our well-known human selfishness, could he solve it? Something else is requisite for the solution. We are none of us aiming at precisely the same mark. With no two men do the points on the target exactly coincide. The most similar of us still aim a hair’s-breadth out of each other’s way, and thus, in the great match, unless intentionally we tread on each others’ kibes, there is room for us all.
If we wished to make a general distinctive classification which, in one way or another, would comprehend our whole humanity, living in its different worlds, there are perhaps no two divisions which would so nearly comprehend it all as those of Ideal and Non-Ideal.

===Chinese===

我们无须统统跪拜在同一座神龛脚下，而是在我们自身的神殿前顶礼膜拜之际，尊重其余所有人的神龛；放下砸毁偶像的铁锤，却亦无须假意在一个庞大平庸的情感偶像面前焚香祷告。

这种包容宽容尚有待我们研习汲取，因为即便在19世纪，它亦绝非一门格外繁盛的美德。我们当今时代的伟大进步，无非是体现在精细化修饰我们偏狭不容异端的方式之上罢了。火刑柱与死牢地牢已被冷嘲与热讽所取代——这种漫骂痛斥的灼痛比前者更为绵长持久，而冷落漠视的致命寒意则远胜于后者。我们尚须侧耳倾听往昔的呼声——那声音自历史的深渊一路回荡至我们当下启蒙与远见的崇高顶峰，在哀戚而赤诚的祷文连祷中永恒呼告：“看在史密斯菲尔德（Smithfield）与罗拉德塔（Lollards’ Tower）的份上，看在抄家充公的贫困与流放异乡的疲惫份上，看在萨伏那洛拉（Savonarola）的鲜血、以及伽利略（Galileo）被幽禁于囚室隔绝其繁星的份上，仁慈些吧——宽容些吧！”

这种庞杂多样的世界格局还带来了一项极佳的硕果，而我们却鲜少予以应有的珍视。试问，清晨漫步在百老汇大道（Broadway）上、穿行在匆匆尘世两大对流之一中的人，谁不曾惊叹：那成千上万奔走匆匆的人群，每个人皆为了自身的生计或享乐而奔忙，却未曾因这种赤裸裸的自私而更加频繁地发生冲撞推搡，人群在彼此穿插渗透时何以未曾引发更为剧烈的摩擦阻力？在事实上，我们目睹他们表现得相当平静、乐于助人且克制自律。倘若将这一难题抛给一位仅仅依据人尽皆知的人类自私数据进行演算的哲学家，他能参透化解吗？解开谜团还需要其余的要素：那便是我们当中绝无任何人瞄准着完全相同的靶心。任意两人在靶标上的落点皆绝不会严丝合缝地重叠。即便是彼此最相仿的我们，瞄准的方向亦彼此相差毫厘；因此，在这场浩瀚的竞技大赛中，除非我们蓄意去踩踏彼此的脚后跟，否则天下之大，自当容得下我们所有人。

倘若我们希望确立一套总体界分的分类体系，以某种方式包罗生活在各自不同世界中的全体人类，那么或许绝无任意两组分类能够比“理想（Ideal）”与“非理想（Non-Ideal）”更为切近地涵盖这一切了。

===Original===

Each of these forms is a Kosmos by itself, which, from its great interior diversity, might, even as thus coaggregated, be properly translated rather system than world; but for our uses the narrower rendering will do, since all the grander laws of each Kosmos are the same for each of its inhabitants.
By these terms, Ideal and Non-Ideal, we mean very much the same ideas that a poet would get from “Visionary” and “Practical;” but these phrases are not of sufficiently catholic interpretation, the former not justly embodying that sneer, nor the latter that praise, which the language of conversation conveys in them.
We have spoken of the intuitional perception as a common ground for all men, limiting, however, the assertion to that branch of the intuitional which has its object in universal truth, and thus meaning that everybody acknowledges an equal force in axioms; and, however we may dispute on other points, all agree that the whole, for instance, is equal to all its parts. Yet there are two other fields of the intuitional, which, so far from their being equally expatiated in by all men, are to some merely known by glimpses, to others, we might almost believe, entirely shut. These are Beauty and Good, higher than Truth, and therefore neither so much needed in our lower affairs, nor so much opened to all mankind by nature or cultivation.
Both the Good and the Beautiful forming each an ideal by itself, for our present purpose we need only treat with the latter. It is in relation to the Beautiful particularly that we wish to exemplify this classification of the Ideal and Non-Ideal.
That beauty is really an ideal, something of the thought inner to us, and not coming in through the passages of the sense from without, is too little perceived in our inaccurate everyday thought, and too little granted even in our moods of calmer philosophy. For this as for so many of our other perversions, we have to thank the sense-theories.
We may examine the matter without a very painful analysis. Treading reverently and softly, as becometh umbrae who intrude upon the privacy of great men, let us steal into Abbotsford, and stand by the chair of Walter Scott, who is looking at a sunset. By his side, upon the floor beneath us, lies that faithful companion of his strolls among the heather, Maida.

===Chinese===

这两种形态中的每一种，本身皆是一座独立的宇宙天地（Kosmos）；因其巨大的内在多样性，即便如此汇聚组合，或许更宜被翻译界定为“体系”而非“世界”；然而对于我们的用途而言，较为狭义的阐释便已足矣，因为每一座宇宙天地的全部崇高法则，对其所有居民而言皆完全同一。

借由“理想”与“非理想”这两个术语，我们所意指的观念，大体正如一位诗人从“幻视者（Visionary）”与“务实者（Practical）”中所领悟到的意涵；然而后一组词汇的解释不够普遍包容，前者未能公允体现世俗口语所传达的讥讽，后者亦未能公允体现其中的褒奖赞誉。

我们曾将直觉感知论述为全人类共通的立足基石，然而却将这一断言严格限定在以普世真理为客体的直觉分支之上，因而意味着人人都承认公理具备同等的效力；且无论我们在其余论点上如何争论不休，所有人皆一致赞同：举例而言，整体等于其各个部分之和。然而，在直觉之中还存在着另外两个领域，它们远非全人类皆能平等驰骋其间——对某些人而言仅能偶获一瞥，对另一些人而言，我们甚至几乎可以相信是彻底紧闭封闭的。这两个领域便是“美（Beauty）”与“善（Good）”；它们高于真理，因此在我们较低级的凡俗事务中既非那般迫切不可或缺，亦未曾通过天性或后天培育向全人类敞开。

鉴于“善”与“美”各自构筑了一套独立的理想境界，针对我们眼下的目的，我们唯独需要探讨后者。正是在关涉“美”的范畴之中，我们尤为期盼去例证“理想”与“非理想”的这一划分体系。

美乃是一种真真切切的理想构想，是某种源于我们内心深处的沉思之物，而绝非经由肉体感官通道从外部涌入之物——这一点在我们不够严谨的日常思维中太少为人所察觉，甚至在我们更为平心静气的哲学思辨中亦太少为人所首肯。对此，一如我们诸多其他的扭曲偏见一样，我们不得不“归功”于感官理论的流毒。

我们无须经过痛苦繁复的剖析便能审视这一课题。且让我们怀着敬畏且轻柔的步履，宛若侵入伟人隐秘私域的随从幻影一般，悄然潜入阿伯茨福德庄园（Abbotsford），伫立在正凝望落日余晖的沃尔特·司各特（Walter Scott）的座椅之旁。在他身旁的脚下地板之上，横卧着那位陪伴他漫步石楠荒原的忠实伴侣——猎犬梅达（Maida）。

===Original===

Since the test we are about to institute demands fairness, we will free our comparison from all imputation of artifice by placing together with the noblest specimen of man the noblest specimen of beast.
Both the poet and the greyhound are looking westward. The same tints fall through the panes upon the faces of both; far up, toward the springs of Tweed, they see the same hills bathed in a dying light, and the clouds that shift above them. Does it surprise us to hear Sir Walter bursting forth in enrapturement; or, truer still, as a meed of the heart to beauty, see him silently gazing toward the sundown with a face which glows and changes, telling more than a thousand lips? But would we be astonished or not if Maida should suddenly give vent to a lyric bark of ecstasy, or even should she refuse to be wheedled from the glories of that view by the whistle of a keeper immemorially associated with dog-meat? Not in the least, you will say; and most people would agree with you; for a hound who appreciated sunsets would be as great a sensation, even in our most nil admirari world, as a cow, who, like Landor, should write feelingly upon green grass, and publish it. He would have the entreé of all literary circles; dinners would be pressed upon him; he would be presented with services of plate; not an album would be without the autograph of this veritable Prince di Canino. Eclipsed in the blinding glory of his éclat, the learned pig would commit suicide by surfeit, and the accomplished fleas end their mortification with their own poison.
But why? A cat may look at a king, why not a dog at a sunset? “Hath not a dog eyes? Hath not a dog paws, organs, dimensions, senses?” Yet, with quite as much astonishment as Shylock asked,

> “Hath a dog money? is it possible

A cur can lend three thousand ducats?”

===Chinese===

鉴于我们即将施行的检验苛求绝对的公允，我们将人类最崇高的典范同走兽中最崇高的典范并置一处，从而使我们的对照彻底免遭任何矫揉造作的指控。

诗人与灵缇犬皆正朝向西方凝望。相同的绚烂霞光穿透窗棂，洒落在人与兽的面庞之上；在遥远前方、朝向特威德河（Tweed）源头的方向，他们目睹了沐浴在残阳余晖中的完全相同的群峦山岭，以及在其上方变幻漂移的层叠云海。听闻沃尔特爵士情不自禁地爆发出狂喜赞叹，抑或更为真切的是——作为心灵对美的无上敬礼——目睹他默默凝视着夕阳残照，面庞泛着光泽且变幻莫测，胜过千百张唇齿的诉说，这难道会令我们感到惊异吗？然而，倘若梅达突然发出一阵抒情狂喜的吠叫，甚或倘若她断然拒绝受训犬员那自古以来便同狗肉相联系的呼哨声所诱惑、执意不愿从那壮丽景致的辉煌中移开目光，我们是否会感到惊愕万分？你定会说：绝不会感到惊异；且绝大多数人皆会赞同你的看法；因为一只懂得鉴赏日落晚霞的猎犬，即便在我们这个最见怪不怪（nil admirari）的世界里，亦会引发一场巨大的轰动，其震撼程度丝毫不亚于一头母牛如同兰多（Landor）那样饱含深情地就青青翠草挥毫著书并公开发表。它将获准自由出入所有文学圈子；各色盛宴将向它纷至沓来；它将被奉赠一整套银质餐具；没有任何一本纪念册里会缺少这位名副其实的“犬界亲王（Prince di Canino）”的亲笔签名。在它那耀眼夺目的光芒映照之下，博学聪敏的算术猪将因暴饮暴食而愤然自尽，而技艺精湛的马戏跳蚤亦将用自身的毒液终结其受辱的余生。

然而这究竟是何缘由？猫尚且可以看国王，狗为何不能看日落？“狗难道没有眼睛吗？狗难道没有爪子、器官、身形、感官吗？”然而，正如夏洛克（Shylock）满怀同样惊愕所发问的那样：

> “狗难道有钱吗？一条恶犬

难道能借给人三千块杜卡特金币？”

===Original===

do we all inquire, “Can a dog see beauty in a sunset?”
Anatomically we dissect his eyes, and (especially if he be a gaze-hound) find them far better calculated than man’s for length and breadth of vision. In all respects they will compare favorably with the same piece of human organism, granting the latter even at the rarest point of development.
Far deeper than any sense lies this subtle appreciation. There is a something in the outer world which does not impress itself on the retina, and of which the mere visual image is but a type. That which delights us is the peculiar essences of things, and the intangible relation of harmony which the essences, manifold in unity, bear to each other and ourselves. In lakes, and mountains, and sky there is beauty to us, because the same Creator lies behind and continues us all. Sprung from the same source, we have a fitness for each other, arising out of the very fact that in our own souls and the world also creative spirit is making itself manifest; in the tangency of the two there is a delightful communion between spirit and spirit, and for the beast this does not exist, since he is not spirit. This very capability which we possess of expressing this communion in language, shows that it is not through sense that the Beautiful flows in, for what can be conceived as more cruel, more in every way unnatural, than that the hound, with senses like our own, should still be dumbly shut up to an impossibility of expression, if, while standing by our side, he was overburdened with the same loveliness as we? The idea is indeed horrible.
Yet doubtless we may wrong the animal upon the other side. Few of us being willing to carry out the sense philosophy to its ultimate conclusions by giving the dog perception of Beauty equally with ourselves, we often go to the opposite extreme, and rather pity him as a being without gratifications beyond the present bone, hearthrug, or exciting chase. He very likely enjoys contemplation as much, proportionally to his kind, as we do. Not the contemplation of the beautiful in nature indeed, but of some other characteristic, which has as true a fitness to his constitution as Beauty has to ours. What this is, of course, from the entire difference of our plane of being, we can only conjecture.

===Chinese===

我们大家皆会发问：“狗能在落日余晖中看见美吗？”

从解剖学角度，我们解剖其眼眸，（尤其是倘若它是一只视觉猎犬的话）会发现在视力所及的纵深与广度上，其构造远比人类更为精妙绝伦。在一切方面，它皆足以同人类机体的同一器官相媲美——纵使假定后者达致了世间最罕见的完备发育水准。

这种精微幽深的鉴赏领悟力，深藏于任何肉体感官之下。在外部世界之中存在着某种实体，它绝不会自行烙印在视网膜之上，而单纯的视觉造像不过是其一种象征符号罢了。真正令我们沉醉欣悦的，乃是万物特有的本质，以及那些在多样性中归于统一的本质在彼此之间以及同我们自身之间所维系的不可名状的和谐纽带。在湖泊、山峦与苍穹之中，之所以对我们存在着美，乃是因为同一位造物主伫立在这一切背后，维系着我们全部的存在。由于发轫于同一源头，我们彼此天然契合，这正是源于在我们的灵魂以及世界之中、造物之灵皆正昭示显现自身这一事实；在两者的相切触碰之中，生发出了精神同精神之间令人沉醉的心灵神会，而对于走兽而言这却绝不存在，因为它并非精神实体。我们所拥有的用语言表达这种神会的能力本身，便证明了“美”绝非经由肉体感官涌入心田的；因为试问世间还有何事能比这样一种设想更为残忍、在方方面面更为违背天理——倘若那只拥有同我们相似感官的猎犬在伫立在我们身旁之际，肩头亦承受着同我们完全相同的壮丽之美所带来的压倒性震撼，却依然只能哑口无言地被紧闭禁锢在绝无可能表达的绝境之中？这一构想委实可怖至极。

然而毫无疑问，我们亦可能在另一端冤枉了生灵动物。鉴于我们当中鲜少有人愿意将感官哲学推演至其终极结论、赋予犬类同我们完全平等的审美感知力，我们便频频走向另一极端，宁可将其怜悯怜恤为一介除了眼前的骨头、壁炉地毯或惊险追逐之外毫无更高享乐的存在。按其自身的物种尺度按比例衡量，它极有可能同我们一样享受着沉思冥想。当然，绝非对大自然之美的沉思，而是对某种其余特质的沉思——该特质对其生理机能的契合程度，正如美对我们机能的契合程度一般真切。鉴于我们存在层面的彻底迥异，这特质究竟为何物，我们当然唯有徒然揣度遐想罢了。

===Original===

It may be something such as this: in the creation there is a capability of sustaining animal life through food, atmosphere, and a variety of means. To us this capability seldom appears except as a logical deduction, in the form of statistics or agricultural history. To the animal it may appear stamped upon all surrounding things; it may be for him the essential truth which they embody, and in trees, herbage, fruitage, he may feel the symbolized principle which prophesies the sustenance of his highest life as our ideals prophesy ours. The Creator, who careth even for sparrows, and will not let them feel themselves unsupported in this great lonely world, may on this lower basis commune with the beast, and by it give him a suggestion of His goodwill toward him, which in his case may be the source of an enjoyment measurably keen with our own.
But through the Beautiful He talks with man only, and to him alone the fitness of the conscious and unconscious creations are expressed in this way. It is a memory of the elder time to be cherished, even though it be the memory of something heard only in dreams, that all men long ago, in ages however primeval, realized Beauty, and answered back its thrill with gladness and hymns. Such a remembrance﻿—yes, if you will say so, even such a dream﻿—is like some not yet extinguished echo of the Creation strophe and antistrophe, when, on the one side, “the morning stars sang together,” and, on the other, “all the sons of God shouted for joy.”
Sadly enough, many of the latter band of singers have been struck dumb since that day. It might be painful to read a census, could we get such a thing, of the persons who love or even recognize Beauty, by itself and for its own unmarketable sake. The bulk of such a document would probably depend upon the style of man who went around through humanity to compile it. A poet would make sad work. His best questions would be so analytic as either to render him unintelligible or obnoxious. At some houses he would be answered, “No, I am no visionary;” and at others, “Clear out! Do you mean to insult me? Can not I see Beauty? Isn’t this a beautiful day, to be sure, with the sun shining so bright that I can get in all my hay?” At all events, he would come home, without having found it necessary to purchase another valise for the conveyance of his papers.

===Chinese===

它或许是这般情形：在受造界之中，存在着借由食物、空气以及各色途径维系动物生命的能力。对我们而言，除了作为统计数据或农业历史形态的逻辑推论之外，这种能力鲜少显现出来。而对走兽而言，它或许深深烙印在环绕其周围的一切事物之上；对它而言，这或许是万物所承载的本质真理，且在树木、草丛与果实之中，它或许能感知到那预示着维系其最高生命的象征法则，一如我们的崇高理想预示着我们的生命一样。那位甚至怜悯麻雀、不忍任由它们在这座浩瀚孤独的世界中感到孤苦无依的造物主，或许正是在这一较低的基石之上同走兽神会往来，并借此向其昭示彰显了他对它的仁善好意——而就走兽而言，这或许构成了其在敏锐深刻程度上完全堪比人类自身享乐的泉源。

然而，造物主唯独借由“美”与人类攀谈，且唯独对人类，有意识与无意识受造界的融洽契合方才以此种方式得以表达。这是一段值得珍视呵护的太古往昔回忆——即便它不过是在梦境中听闻的回响：在极其久远的太古洪荒岁月里，所有人皆能真切体悟到“美”，并以欢欣雀跃与激昂颂歌回应其激荡震撼。这般回忆——诚然，倘若你愿这般称呼，哪怕这般幻梦——宛若创世之初那尚未彻底熄灭的合唱与应答回响：在那时，一侧是“晨星一同歌唱”，另一侧则是“神的众子皆欢呼雀跃”。

极其令人哀戚的是，自那一刻起，后一支歌者队伍中的诸多成员便已然陷入了失语沉默。倘若我们能够获取这样一份人口普查台账——清点那些能够单纯为了美自身及其不可买卖的纯粹价值而热爱、乃至仅能辨识出“美”的人数——读来该是何等令人扼腕痛惜。这样一份文献的厚度，大概完全取决于周游人世去编纂它的究竟是何许人也。一位诗人定会把这项差事办得一塌糊涂。他最深刻的发问定会因过于精微剖析而要么使人不知所云，要么令人心生厌恶。在某些门户前他会碰壁得到这般回答：“不，我绝非什么不切实际的幻视者；”而在另一些门前则是：“滚开！你想羞辱我吗？我难道看不出美？今天艳阳高照、正好把我的干草全部收拢进仓，难道还不算是个大美天吗？”无论如何，他在归家之时，断无任何必要再去额外购置一只皮箱来搬运其调研卷宗了。

===Original===

Whatever may be the reason, it can not be doubted that there is a great difference between men in the appreciation of the subtle characteristic, and in some it seems to be entirely lacking. There is one class of men who exult in beauty, who live in it, whose extreme representatives are willing even to commit all sorts of practical extravagances for its sake. There is another, whose members look at a statue of Phidias, and then at a gatepost, and in both see only something hard, white, and tall.
Yet they both have to live in what is geographically the same world. It is of the ideal man, as representative of the former class, and of some of his relations to that world, that we have to speak. A greater breadth of these relations than might at first sight be supposed is included in the question. Why do ideal men often use narcotics? Indisputably it is ideal men. The fact is there, however great a pity it may be. Let us seek, for a while, an answer to the question.
The wants of the ideal man, while in number less than those of his opposite, in degree are far greater. Dives, as the type of the pure worldly life, is as incapable as guiltless of those vague, unsatisfied longings which he so much censures in a neighbor and discourages in a dependent. All things out of which he can extort pleasure coin themselves for him in a perfectly tangible shape. He is fully satisfied, his wishes need no additional fulfillment to make a complete orb, if his balance strikes accurately at the counting-room, if he can go home behind his own horses when too tired to walk, his dinner is good, his wife handsome, his house comfortable, his daughters well settled, his sons imitating their father. All these requisitions he can lay his hand on; if he could not, his longings would not be vague; he would know what he wanted, and, under ordinary circumstances, could get it in time.
Ariel, on the other hand, is contented with a catalogue of enjoyments in numerical and money value far less. It was not he who originated that sneer upon love in a cottage. He was filled with infinitely more than the mere satisfaction of their material by the woodbine which clambered around his windows, the roses leading from the doorstep to the gate, the lake below him, the mountains on the other side, the fruit and the loaf upon his table, and the other cleanly and kindly answers to his domestic needs.

===Chinese===

无论缘由究竟为何，无可置疑的是：世人在对这种精微特质的鉴赏领悟上存在着巨大的鸿沟，而在某些人身上它似乎彻底匮乏。世间存在着这样一类人：他们在美之中欢欣沉醉，在美之中生息繁衍，其极端代表人物甚至甘愿为了美去犯下各色世俗实务上的挥霍奢靡。而另有一类人：其成员端详着菲迪亚斯（Phidias）雕琢的塑像，随后又望了望门前的一根门柱，在两件物什上皆仅仅看见了某种坚硬、发白且高耸之物。

然而，他们双方却皆不得不生活在地理意义上完全相同的同一座世界之中。我们所要探讨的，正是作为前一类人代表的“理想之人”，以及他同那一世界所维系的若干纽带。而在如下这一发问之中，便囊括了比乍看之下所能预想到的更为宽博深远的关联纽带：为何理想之人频频沾染使用麻醉剂？无可争辩的是，使用它们的正是理想之人。无论这是何等巨大的憾事，铁一般的事实就摆在眼前。且让我们暂且去探寻这一问题的答案。

理想之人的渴求，纵然在数量上要少于其对立面，然而在剧烈程度上却要深沉巨大得多。作为纯粹世俗生活典型的财主（Dives），对于他在邻人身上严加斥责、在仆从身上百般压抑的那种朦胧迷茫且无法满足的焦灼渴望，既无从体会亦绝无罪愆。一切他能从中榨取享乐之物，皆以绝对真切可见的形态为他铸就呈现。倘若他在账房里的账目结算得分毫不差，倘若疲惫懒于步行之际能由自家的骏马拉着归家，倘若晚餐精美丰盛、妻子容貌俊俏、宅邸舒适惬意、女儿皆已妥帖出阁、儿子皆步其父亲之后尘，他便心满意足，其心愿断无须任何额外的填补便已达致圆满的明珠之境。所有这些要求他皆能触手可及；即便未能如愿，他的渴盼亦绝非含混朦胧；他心知肚明自己究竟渴求何物，且在寻常境况下假以时日终能如愿以偿。

反观爱丽儿（Ariel），则满足于在数量与金钱价值上微薄得多的享乐清单。对“茅舍之恋（love in a cottage）”的冷嘲热讽绝非出自他的唇齿。攀附在窗棂周围的金银花（woodbine），从门阶逶迤延展至院门的玫瑰花丛，脚下的大湖，对岸的峰峦，餐桌上的鲜果与面包，以及对家庭生计需求的一应洁净而温馨的回应，皆赋予了他远非物质单纯满足所能企及的浩瀚充盈。

===Original===

But the tax-gatherer came to spy out the land, the insatiate genius of mill-building looked at the brook which ran by his garden, and pronounced it a “location.”
Presently the waters began to run foul with dye and sawdust, gigantic band-wheels spun and hummed where birds had sung; there was a creaking, a dust, a baleful fire night and day, which invaded his library and his dreams. Provisions rose; the simplest fare upon which he had kept together soul and body now stood just where his labors could reach it upon tiptoe.
So strongly, while it does cling, does the body pull upon the soul, that, though we may be spiritually happy without being sumptuous, we can not, at the same time, be spiritual and hungry. At least most of us can not. Into what a glory, looked at through such a fact, does the Massinger tower who, with one hand stiffly holding the wolf at arm’s length, with the other can indite the Virgin Martyr. Yes, there have been some such souls after all.
But our Ariel, being of less muscular make, is not among them. His “mind to him a kingdom is,” but he is expatriated from it on a foraging expedition; through the jaws of Scylla and Charybdis, starvation on the one hand, and the premature old age of overwrought energies, he is voyaging in a supply-ship. If even now he could sit still in an occasional lull, and grow better by drinking in beauty, and make other men happier by imparting it to them through words, writing, or kindly offices, he sees only money-utility stamped upon the rivers, and the whole face of nature is staked off into building-lots or manufactory-sites. The features of his goddess have become the “desirable features” for a paper-speculation town.
There are a thousand ways in which his neighbors can evaporate the essence which is all in all to him, while they at the same time give to his scenery a ponderable value which to them is worth far more.
Perhaps, like Southey, he now out and out curses the mills. But this is wrong; for Southey, though a noble poet in spite of the insolence of Byron, was still no great political economist, notwithstanding the opinion of himself. Perhaps, therefore, he only sighs, and moves his household gods to another hearth﻿—it may be where loneliness will better secure him from disturbance, it may be where labors of his particular kind yield fuller sustenance to the crying wants of life.

===Chinese===

然而税务官前来进行土地测绘勘查，兴建作坊工场的贪婪精魂盯上了流经他花园的小溪，断言其为一处绝佳的“厂址”。

不久，溪水开始因染料与木屑锯末而变得浑浊污秽；巨大的传动皮带轮在昔日鸟雀啼鸣之处飞速旋转轰鸣；日日夜夜充斥着令人烦躁的摩擦声、尘土与阴森的炉火，侵扰侵蚀着他的书斋与清梦。粮饷百物飞涨；维系其肉躯与灵魂不散的最简朴粗茶淡饭，如今已然攀升至唯有踮起脚尖劳作方能勉强够着的高度。

当肉躯尚且攀附羁绊之际，它对灵魂的拉扯撕扯是如此剧烈：纵然我们在精神上无须奢华铺张亦能怡然自得，然而我们却绝无法在饱受饥肠辘辘折磨的同时保持超凡脱俗的精神。至少我们绝大多数人断然无法做到。透过这一现实加以审视，马辛格（Massinger）的身姿该是何等辉煌巍峨啊——他能一手吃力地将恶狼拒于一臂之外，另一手却依然能挥毫写就《童贞女殉道者》（*Virgin Martyr*）。诚然，人世间终究曾存在过若干这般伟大的灵魂。

然而我们的爱丽儿，身形骨骼未免过于柔弱纤细，断非此类坚忍之辈。纵然“心智于他便是一座王国”，他却不得不流亡在外四处觅食奔波；穿行于斯库拉（Scylla）与卡律布狄斯（Charybdis）的险滩恶浪之间——一侧是饥寒交迫的死亡深渊，另一侧则是劳碌透支精魂所致的未老先衰——他正坐在一艘补给船上艰难涉险。纵使在此时此刻，他偶能借着风平浪静的喘息之机安坐片刻，借由吮吸美感而升华心智，并通过言辞、笔墨或善行将其分润世人以造福苍生，然而他所目睹的，却唯有被烙印在江河川流之上的金钱功利算计，而大自然的整个面庞皆已被打桩圈占为建筑地块或工场作坊厂址。他心目中女神的圣洁容颜，已沦为纸面投机市镇图纸上所谓的“宜人卖点”。

他的邻人们拥有上千种手段去挥发蒸发掉于他而言便是一切的万物精髓，与此同时却给他的山川景致赋予了某种沉重有形的世俗价值——而这在他们眼中远比其余一切都更为值钱。

或许，他会如同骚塞（Southey）那样，彻底指着那些作坊工场破口大骂。然而这绝非明智之举；因为骚塞尽管不顾拜伦（Byron）的傲慢无礼依然算得上一位崇高的诗人，但正如其对自身的评价一样，他终究算不上什么伟大的政治经济学家。因此，爱丽儿或许唯有幽幽叹息，将自家的守护神（household gods）搬迁至另一座炉灶之旁——或许迁往孤寂能更好庇护他免受侵扰的僻壤，或许迁往其特有的劳作能够更充裕地填补生计嗷嗷待哺之需的居所。

===Original===

The pangs of such a moving are little known to anyone but himself, or, if he has God’s crowning gift in a deep-feeling and congenial wife, to her alone beside him. The men of the world can not hear the groans of the uprooted mandrakes.
There is the hilltop, upon which, first of all visible things, his eyes for so many years have lighted in awaking. It has grown to be to him the only summit over which it could be conceived possible for the sun to rise. There is the lake along whose shores he has led his children, calling them to watch its hues and dimples at evening; along those same shores, mayhap, his father led him. Every tree, as far as the skirt of the horizon, is known to him; he has wandered over every slope; he has dreamed or written suggestions in his notebook upon every crag. The whole scenery has been to him his school, his gymnasium, his holiday-ground. He must leave it all.
And his house﻿—it was there that he felt upon his forehead, in blessing, the hands of the now long dead; here, many a year ago, he knew man’s only peace except death, childhood﻿—knew it for a little time, while his locks were sunny and the grave shadows yet tarried from his face﻿—then vanished it away. Hither he led home his new-made wife; here, “into something rich and strange,” blossomed that mystic, intangible relation of delights when a child was born to the bosoms which are twain, yet one; here, with his children, in the firelight gambols he kindled the dampened torch of the younger time, and for one evening was a child again.
Here, too, is his library﻿—that cave in the rock above the world’s high tide, set farther in than the surges beat or the winds blow. The tide has reached it now. There are waves and seaweed on the floor at flood﻿—they do not all go out at ebb. Where can he read but at that window? Where can he write but on that desk and against that wall? How can the old familiar animus of the place be left behind, unless his own soul, which had grown its twin, stays with it? Yet how can the animus be transported? No, no, it can not. It knows no luggage trains; it is not a thing of drays.
Everywhere the tentacles of his root must give way with a wrench; the necessity being granted, the pain is inevitable; the only remedy, a manly patience under the irremediable﻿—the

> “Quicquid corrigere est nefas.”

===Chinese===

这般迁徙的撕心裂肺之痛，除了他自己之外旁人知之甚少；抑或倘若他得蒙上帝的无上眷顾拥有深情相知且志趣相投的妻子，便唯有身旁的她能感同身受。世故之辈是断然听不见被连根拔起的风茄（mandrakes）所发出的哀号悲泣的。

看那山巅，多少年来每逢梦醒之际，他的双眸第一眼触碰到的有形之物便是它。它在他心中已然化作了唯一能让红日喷薄升起的崇高之峰。看那湖畔，他曾沿着湖岸牵着儿女的小手，呼唤他们凝眸端详暮色中华美的波光涟漪；或许在那同一处湖畔，他的父亲昔日亦曾这般牵引着幼年的他。极目所至、直至地平线尽头的每一棵树木，皆是他熟稔的故交；他曾漫步丈量过每一道山坡；在每一座巉岩峭壁之上，他皆曾托腮冥想或在笔记本上记下吉光片羽的灵思。整座山川景致于他而言，便是他的学堂，他的练兵场，他的休假乐园。而如今，他必须将这一切全盘抛诸身后。

还有他的宅邸——正是在彼处，他曾感到如今早已作古的长辈将祝福的手掌轻按在他的额头之上；正是在此地，多年前他曾品尝过除死亡之外人类唯一的安宁——童年；在那段短暂的时光里，他拥有一头金黄如阳的发丝，肃穆的阴翳尚未爬上面庞——随后童年便烟消云散了。正是在这里，他迎娶了新婚的爱妻；正是在这里，当一个稚子诞生在这对虽为两人却合而为一的怀抱中时，那神秘不可言喻的无尽欢欣纽带绽放出了“奇异而绚烂（rich and strange）”的光华；正是在这里，在炉火辉映的嬉戏打闹之中，他同儿女们重新点燃了早年间早已受潮的火炬，并在某个夜晚重新化为一介孩童。

还有他的书斋——那座高悬于尘世滔天浊浪之上的岩壁洞穴，深藏在远非狂澜所能拍击、罡风所能吹卷的僻静深处。而如今，大潮已然漫过了门槛。涨潮之时地板上泛着波涛与海藻——退潮之际它们却未能全数退尽。除了那扇明窗，他还能坐在何处研读？除了那张书案与那面墙壁，他又能在何处伏案挥毫？除非将他自身那已然与之结为孪生的灵魂割舍留存下来，否则那弥漫在此地的熟悉气韵与精魄（animus）又怎能轻易割舍？然而那一缕精魄又怎能搬运迁移？不能，绝不能。它不知晓何为行李列车；它绝非大车板车所能拉运装载之物。

他的根须触角必须处处经历撕扯剥离之痛；既然迁徙已成定局，痛楚便不可避免；唯一的解药，唯有在不可挽回的命数面前展现出一介大丈夫的坚忍克制——那便是：

> “不可挽回之命数，唯有坚忍承受（Quicquid corrigere est nefas）。”

===Original===

Now if he were to tell Dives all this, taking him into his confidence, would he not laugh? “What is the sense,” would be the reply of that satisfied person, “in fashing your beard about one place or another? If you are going to town, you will probably take a far better house than this trumpery cottage﻿—four stories high, freestone front, all the modern improvements, and eligible situation. Why, my dear sir, you must be mad! Think what an exchange﻿—gas instead of spermaceti; bathing apparatus, with warm, cold, and shower cocks, instead of this portable concern you have here, or perhaps instead of a mere swim in your twopenny lake; the market within ten minutes’ staging; shopping conveniences for your wife; a daily laid, still wet, on your doorstep﻿—everything imaginable, in place of this uncultivated, mountainous, windy, woody situation, out of call of express-wagons and solid respectability. Or, if you are going still farther into the woods (which, I own, is very foolish, since you might stay here and put up a sawmill on your own part of the brook, which would make you one of our first manufacturers), there is still no such cause as you seem to think for sorrow at moving. Probably, where you will settle, vegetables and all provisions are far cheaper﻿—you can get your wood for almost nothing﻿—and certainly those are advantages that a man need not pull a long face over. Be a man. Satisfy yourself with the world, as I do.”
Ah! unction not in Ariel’s pharmacopoeia! He is hurt where such salves will not heal him.
In many a way may the sources of his enjoyment be dried up or imbittered which the world knows not of. The ideal nature is indeed a harp of many wondrous strings, but the airs that play upon it in this life are seldom of the gentlest. The one-stringed Hawaiian guitar of the non-ideal man is easily thrummed, and never lacks tone save when its proper backbone of material well-being is temporarily lax.
If any of us, even the most tender and spiritually appreciative, could understand the various intensity with which this law works out its office in other men of the same nature, we would be much kinder in our judgment of the man who runs to narcotics and other stimulants for relief, while we regarded the habit as no less grievous.

===Chinese===

如今，倘若他将这一切向财主全盘托出、引为知己，对方难道不会嗤之以鼻、抚掌大笑吗？“为了一处地方或另一处地方而愁白胡须，究竟有何意义可言？”那位心满意足之辈定会这般回答，“倘若你迁往市镇，你大概能住上一座远胜这间破旧茅舍的豪宅——足足四层楼高、砂岩饰面外墙、一应现代化改良设施应有尽有、地段优越宜人。哎呀，我亲爱的先生，你定是疯了！想想看这是何等划算的互换——燃气煤气取代了鲸蜡油灯；配备温水、冷水与淋浴龙头的沐浴设施，取代了你眼前这台笨拙的便携式木盆，抑或取代了在你那不值两个子儿的破湖里单纯泡澡；乘马车只需十分钟便能直达集市菜场；为尊夫人提供各色便捷的采买便利；一份墨迹未干的晨报每日清晨稳妥地送到门阶之上——应有尽有，何苦留恋这处未开垦、荒山野岭、狂风呼啸、林木丛生、快递大车与体面阶层皆绝难涉足的蛮荒之地。抑或，倘若你打算进一步迁入更深的山林之中（坦白讲，这未免太过愚蠢，毕竟你原本大可留在此地，在属于你自己的小溪河段上架设一座锯木作坊，这足以使你跻身我们当地首屈一指的制造商之列），亦绝无你所设想的那般因搬迁而哀戚悲泣的因由。在你定居之地，蔬菜粮饷大概要廉价得多——木柴几乎不花分文便可随意砍伐——凡此种种，确凿无疑皆是一个人大可不必拉长了苦脸的大好优势。拿出个男子汉的气概来吧。如同我一样，在这座世界上好好安享满足吧。”

啊！这种膏油绝不在爱丽儿的药典之中！他在内心深处所遭受的创伤，绝非此类油膏所能抚平愈合。

他的享乐源泉可以通过世故之辈一无所知的上千种途径被汲干枯竭或化作苦涩。理想天性委实是一架系着无数奇妙琴弦的竖琴，然而在这尘世生命之中抚弄它的长风，却鲜少温和柔顺。反观非理想之人的单弦夏威夷吉他，弹奏起来轻而易举，且只要物质安康的坚实脊梁骨未曾暂时松弛，其音调便永无匮乏走调之虞。

倘若我们当中的任何人——哪怕是最温柔且在精神上最具领悟鉴赏力之人——能够领会这一法则在其余同类天性之人身上究竟以何等纷繁的剧烈程度施行其运作，我们在评判那些奔向麻醉剂与其他兴奋剂寻求慰藉解脱之人时便会宽厚仁慈得多，即便我们依然认为这种习癖本身同样沉重悲戚。

===Original===

Could we, for example, enter thoroughly into the constitution of such a one as Coleridge; could we realize his temptations to the full extent; understand his struggles, and weigh all the forces of the mind which gave him, from his very birth, a perilous tendency, how much oftener with tears than with denunciations of his indolence, his neglect of duties, would we read such memorials of him as have been published, much as the most of those seem directed to bias us in the contrary way.
For it seems as if there has never been a real “Life” of Coleridge. We have had, in abundance, sketches of what he himself might have called his “phenomenal existence.” We have the changes of place which he made; the towns in which he lectured; the letters from home which he did not open, and the correspondences for aid in starvation which he did open; the worth, in pounds sterling, of the laudanum which he drank per week; the number of bottles of brandy which he emptied in the same time; the extravagances of his expenditure; his repentings, his concessions to Southey and Cottle. All these are phenomenal﻿—yea, even the last three. We have external events﻿—movements of which we do not see the motor. Perhaps it would be impossible to see it from anything but an autobiography so full, so ab intra, that pain and humiliation would deter him from writing it, were he living. This would be a “Life” of Coleridge; the others are mere results of that life.
Perhaps the best substitute for such a work is to be found in his brief and fragmentary prose works; for, although they have almost nothing of that narrative style which is supposed to be necessary to the legitimate memoirs, they still show us, to a degree unequaled by anything extant, Coleridge, the Man and the Mind.
A man he was to whom the world of his imagination and his reason was far more than that wherein he reaped his honors and his daily bread.

===Chinese===

举例而言，倘若我们能够彻底洞悉诸如柯勒律治（Coleridge）这般人物的深层心理构造；倘若我们能完全体认他所承受的诱惑试探；能够参透其内心的激烈抗争，并权衡从其呱呱坠地起便赋予他危险倾向的全部心智力量，那么我们在翻阅业已出版的关于他的诸多纪念生平传记之时，定会频繁地潸然泪下，而非痛斥其懈怠懒惰与擅离职守——尽管绝大多数传记似乎皆旨在将我们引向相反的偏见轨道。

因为世间似乎从未有过一部关于柯勒律治的真正“生平传记”。关于他自己或许会称之为“现象界存在”的生平速写，我们早已屡见不鲜、汗牛充栋。我们掌握着他辗转迁徙的居所变动；他发表演说的各座市镇；他未曾拆封的家书，以及在饥肠辘辘中寻求救济而确实拆封的信函往来；他每周所吞服鸦片酊折合的英镑价值；他在同一时期内饮尽排空的白兰地酒瓶数量；其挥霍无度的开销开支；他的痛心忏悔，以及他对骚塞与科特尔（Cottle）的退让妥协。所有这些皆属现象界的皮相——是的，哪怕最后三项亦复如是。我们目睹了外部事件——那些我们无法洞悉其原动力的表层运动。或许除了撰写一部如此详尽充沛、全然发自肺腑深处（ab intra）以至于痛楚与羞辱会阻遏他在世时提笔挥毫的自传之外，绝无法看清那股原动力。唯有那样的著作方才称得上柯勒律治真正的“生平”；而其余一切皆不过是那一生命的表象结果罢了。

或许替代此类著作的最佳替代品，正是见诸其短小而零散的散文篇章之中；因为，纵然它们几乎丝毫不具备被视作正统回忆录所必需的叙事体例风格，却依然以世间现存任何文献皆无法比拟的深刻程度，向我们昭示了柯勒律治其人与他的心智。

他乃是这样一个人：对其而言，由其想象与理性所构筑的世界，其分量远胜于他赖以斩获世俗荣光与日常糊口口粮的尘世凡间。

===Original===

Sensitive as a child to that intangible yet infinite meaning which is expressed in frowns and smiles, in love, scorn, and neglect; by nature gifted with an insight into her excellencies which cultivation and the other circumstances of his progressive being made at times even morbidly acute; living, by the very necessity of his particular inborn law of life, at the very summit of his energies, he had worn out nerve and elasticity at an age when, according to all ordinary judgments which base themselves on insurance averages and statistics of longevity, he should have been in the prime of his life, and battling his way with fortitude to a competency. He exhausted by mighty drafts all his credit at the bank of healthful life, and that is a corporation which never permits us to overdraw. Up to the very last deposit of blood and sinew, nerve and spirit, prompt payment will meet every demand; then comes the crash, and the bankrupt nature is no longer known on ’Change. If all that we know of Coleridge from without, the statement of himself and his contemporaries, did not intimate to us that such was the case with him, we might determine that it would necessarily happen so, *a priori*, from that which we know of the mental constitution of the man.
He tells us through his memorializer, Cottle, and the others who have written about him, that he first used opium as a remedy for disease﻿—a painful disease of the legs﻿—that he found its effects a delicious and perfect relief. Furthermore, that he abandoned it with the completion of his cure, but resumed it upon his finding, with the abandonment, the pains return. That he made several attempts to free himself with the same termination, and at last settled down into the opium-eater which he was for it is impossible to say how many years of his life.
All this we have no reason to doubt. As an alleviative to severe pain the narcotic first became known to him. Yet the secret of its excessive use, the rapidly increasing doses, beyond all the demands of the body for relief﻿—what was that? Ah! the poet himself would confess that to his mind the indulgence spoke with a fascination far greater than to his physical nature. It was, in fact, the very thing necessary for the replenishment of his exhausted capability of enjoyment.
How is it, we must ask, that opium acts upon the whole organism of a susceptible man?

===Chinese===

如同一介稚童般敏感于颦蹙与笑容、挚爱、蔑视与冷落中所表达的不可名状却无限深沉的意蕴；在天性上得天独厚地被赋予了对万物卓越绝伦之处的深刻洞察力，而后天的教养与循序渐进生存境遇的其余因素更是将这种洞察力打磨至时而近乎病态般敏锐的境地；受制于其特定与生俱来的生命法则之必然，他始终生活在自身精魂能量的最巅峰水准，以至于在一个依照立足于保险平均寿命与寿算统计数据的寻常判断而言本应正值壮年、理当坚毅奋斗以求博得殷实生计的年岁里，他便已然耗尽了自身的神经与生命韧性。借由浩瀚的透支提款，他挥霍空了自己在健康生命银行里的全部信用额度，而那是一家绝不容许我们超额透支的严苛财阀。直至耗尽最后一滴血液与筋骨、神经与精魄的存款之前，即时支付尚能满足一切挥霍索求；随后便是彻底的轰然崩盘，而那宣告破产的体魄在交易所（’Change）上从此无人问津。即便我们从外部所知晓的关于柯勒律治的一切、以及他自身同侪的陈述未曾向我们昭示他身上的这一实情，单单依据我们对该人心智构造的认知，我们亦能先验（a priori）判定其结局必然如此。

他借由其纪念传记作者科特尔以及其余撰写其生平的诸人告诉我们：他最初之所以沾染鸦片，乃是将其作为治疗病患的解药——一种发生在腿部的剧痛顽疾——且他发觉其药效带来了一种美妙绝伦且彻底圆满的抚慰解脱。此外，随着病痛的痊愈他曾一度弃绝了该药物，然而却发觉一旦停药剧痛便卷土重来，于是只得重操旧药。他曾数次尝试摆脱药瘾的羁绊，却无一例外皆以相同的结局告终，最终彻底沦为了一介食鸦片者，其吸食年岁之漫长难以估量。

凡此种种，我们毫无理由心存怀疑。这种麻醉剂最初确实是作为剧烈痛苦的镇痛剂才为人所熟知的。然而，其过量滥用、剂量飞速激增、乃至远远凌驾于肉躯寻求阵痛解脱的全部实际需求之上的隐秘症结——究竟何在？啊！诗人自己定会坦陈：该药剂对他的心智所散发出的致命诱惑力，远甚于对其肉体机能的吸引。在事实上，这恰恰是补充填补其枯竭耗尽的享乐能力所不可或缺的神药。

我们必须追问：鸦片究竟是如何作用于一个敏感之人的全部机体之上的？

===Original===

Physically the books of medicine tell us how﻿—that is, to a certain distance they mark its pathway through digestion, circulation, the sympathetic nerves, and, where it causes death, leave it in an engorgement of the brain.
Probably all these phenomena are the merely external ones; they do not at all give us the mode of its action, after all. At one time, in the course of some experiments, I thought I had reached a little deeper principle of its operation; some singular facts led me to form a theory upon the subject. I will not give it here, since there is not yet a basis of tests broad enough for it to rest upon philosophically. Of all specific actions, that of narcotics is conceded by physicists universally to be one of the most recondite. Hardly anything is really known about it by the practitioner more than by his unscientific patient. We have mere facts ungrouped about their governing principle.
But mentally we know its working better. Opium supersedes, and, by long continuance in its indulgence, actually extirpates that vital force out of which arise hope, insight into excellencies, fortitude, volition, and volition made permanent in perseverance. It is an artificial energy destructive to all natural; men habituated to it live on when what is called the nervous life is perfectly extinct.
That Coleridge could not have continued to live at all without such energy in some form is evident from the whole constitution of the man. Without the ever-present sensitive perception of spiritual beauty for which such an energy was necessary, house, lands, comfortable family arrangements, the remunerative place in the Quarterly which his friends procured him would have seemed mere *idola*. He hungered and thirsted for the spiritual. The world of dreams which he had built up in his “Pantisocracy” had been exhaled under the pressure of daily-bread necessities when to his fortuneless bosom he took a portionless wife. It is impossible that such a nature as his, emptied of the ideal Utopia, should be long void of something else as ideal. And so through all his life we see him forgetting hunger in dreams till it bites him to the heart. Then he starts up to spasmodic exertion, to sleep again in visions when the foe is driven to a respectful distance. Call this wrong, call it undutiful to relationships which he was bound to respect, yet you can not call it indolence.

===Chinese===

在肉体生理层面上，医学典籍告知了我们答案——也就是说，在一定限度内，它们标示描摹了药物穿透消化系统、血液循环、交感神经的路径，且在其致人死亡之处，终结于大脑的充血淤血。

或许所有这些现象皆不过是纯粹外部的皮相罢了；它们归根结底根本未能向我们揭示其作用机理的本质模态。在某个时期的一系列实验过程之中，我曾以为自己触碰到了其运作的某种更深层原理；某些奇异的事实诱导我确立了一套关于该课题的理论。我在此不会将其铺陈展开，因为尚无足够宽广的实验测试基石足以让其在哲学上稳固立足。在一切特异作用之中，麻醉剂的作用被物理学者普遍公认为最深奥晦涩的谜团之一。执业医官对此的真正认知，几乎并不比其毫无科学常识的病患多出分毫。我们手中不过掌握着一群未曾依循其统治法则加以归类的零散事实罢了。

然而在心智心理层面上，我们对其运作机理却了然于胸。鸦片取代了——且借由长期的沉溺吸食，实则彻底连根拔除了——孕育生发出希望、卓越洞察力、坚韧毅力、意志，以及经由坚守转化为持久执念的意志所依托的那股生命元气活力。它乃是一种足以摧毁一切天然能量的人工造作能量；习惯于此的人，即便在所谓的神经生命彻底熄灭之后，依然能苟延残喘维系生存。

从柯勒律治整个人的心理构造中便昭然若揭：倘若未曾以某种形式获取此类能量，他根本绝无可能维系生存。倘若匮乏那种必须依凭此类能量方能维系的对属灵之美的时刻敏感感知，宅邸、地产、惬意的家庭起居安排，以及友人为他在《季度评论》（*Quarterly*）谋得的优厚差事，在他眼中皆不过是虚妄的“偶像（idola）”。他如饥似渴地渴慕着精神属灵之物。当他在身无长物之际将一位毫无嫁妆的妻子迎入怀抱之时，他在其“大同乌托邦（Pantisocracy）”中所构筑的梦幻世界，便在糊口生计的重压之下烟消云散了。如他这般的天性，在被掏空了理想的乌托邦之后，绝无可能长期任由心头空无其余同样理想之物的填补。因此，纵观其毕生岁月，我们目睹他在幻梦之中忘却了饥寒交迫，直至饥肠辘辘如毒蛇般噬咬其心头。随后他惊起做出痉挛性的挣扎奋进，而一旦将强敌驱逐至礼貌的安全距离之外，便又重新在梦幻景象中安然酣眠。责难这是一种过错吧，斥责这是对本该尊重的家庭名分的不负责任吧，然而你却绝无法将其蔑称为懈怠懒惰。

===Original===

He was not fitted by nature to do the work of a material life, yet higher obligations called him to change his element, and he should have obeyed, against nature. In his own world he was a diligent, a glorious worker﻿—he was not indolent﻿—he only wrought out life according to his tendency, his constitutional fitness, and there he sinned.
Yet, oh man of the world! you who are so ready to sneer at Coleridge, let the comparison between him and yourself be put upon fair grounds before we join you in denunciation of the sin. The way in which you state the comparison is this: “Here am I, fighting the world in its roughest forms for a livelihood; there was Coleridge, who would not brace his muscles and fight like me.” In another way let us state the case. Suppose yourself and Coleridge translated to that spiritual world where there are no actualities of the precise kind which you cope with. Grant that you each retained the same natural constitution as on earth, with how much ease or willingness would you change your element and labor in his province? It would then be his right to be called the actor; you would be the dreamer, and your dreams would be of things which as little suited his everyday activity as in this world his suited your own. You would be called with stentorian voices to awake to the reality of things﻿—to dismiss the visionary figments of commerce, manufactures, credit, and capital, and to strive boldly in the arena of thought and art, and other spiritual excellences.
Do you say, “But every man’s business is with the world in which he is placed for the time being?” I acknowledge that; but it is a misfortune, an imperfection of the present state. The greatest harmony is that wherein every mind works out most fully its own office. Still, the higher obligation, the moral, called Coleridge to an uncongenial activity, and in not going he was wrong. Remember in an analogous case what you would do; think what a hard thing is the change of element, and then denounce if you can.
Interpreting the opium passage of Coleridge’s life by marginal references from all the pages previous, we shall see him more justly, and therefore more gently than by any light thrown upon it as an isolated paragraph, from severe commentaries framed according to a personal and unappreciative standard.
We shall see him first as the boy.

===Chinese===

他在天性上便不适宜从事物质生活的劳碌苦工，然而更为崇高的道德天职呼唤他去改变自身的生存环境要素，且他本该违背天性予以顺从听命。在他自己的世界里，他是一介勤勉、辉煌的劳作者——他绝非懈怠懒惰——他仅仅是依循自身的性情偏好与生理心理契合度去践行生命，而正是在此处，他犯下了罪愆过失。

然而，世故之人啊！你们这些如此热衷于冷嘲热讽柯勒律治的人，在我们加入你们一同声讨其罪愆之前，且让我们将他与你们自身的对照置于公允平等的基石之上吧。你们陈述对照的方式乃是这般：“瞧瞧我，正在为了糊口生计而在世间最粗粝的环境中博弈厮杀；再看看柯勒律治，他却不愿紧绷筋骨同我一般拼杀。”且让我们换一种方式来陈述这一境况：假定将你与柯勒律治一同迁徙传送至那座精神世界——在彼处绝无任何与你当下所周旋应付完全相同的具体现实。假定你们各自保留着同在尘世间完全相同的天然生理心理构造，你又能拥有何等从容或心甘情愿去改变自身的生存环境要素、在其专属的领地疆域内耕耘劳作？届时，享有被称为实干行动者权利的将是他；而你将沦为一介做梦的空想家，且你所梦见之物与他的日常作为格格不入的程度，一如在眼下的尘世间他的作为与你的行事格格不入一般。世人将以洪钟般的高亢嗓音呼唤你从沉睡中苏醒、去面对事物的真正现实——呼唤你抛弃关于商业、制造业、信用与资本的虚妄空想织造物，并在思想、艺术以及其余精神卓越的高迈竞技场上奋勇拼搏。

你会反驳道：“然而每个人的本分，皆在于应付他当下所置身的世界啊？”我承认这一点；然而这乃是当下境况的不幸与不完备之处。最崇高的和谐，莫过于每一个心智皆能最淋漓尽致地践行其专属的天职。尽管如此，那更为崇高、更为道德的天职呼唤柯勒律治去投身于一场同其天性不合的活动，而他未能奔赴前去，这确实是他的过错。但在类似的处境下，请扪心自问你又当如何抉择；设想一下彻底改变生存环境要素是何等艰难痛苦的创举，随后，倘若你尚能开口，再去严加斥责吧。

借助柯勒律治生平前文各页的边缘注脚来诠释其生命中吞服鸦片的这一章节，我们将比那些依循个人偏见与毫无领悟鉴赏力的严苛评注、将其作为孤立段落投射审视的视角，更为公允、因而亦更为温柔宽厚地端详审视他。

首先，且让我们端详少年时代的柯勒律治。

===Original===

The child, as the cut-and-dried biographies have it, of poor but highly respectable parentage, that very strict economy which is so erroneously supposed to educate families into practical habits, cultivated his ideal tendency until it became exquisite. The necessity of a careful use of means in a household is the last of all things to rear its children practically. Extreme poverty, no doubt, from stimulating the very primitive activities of existence, may make a progeny which is intellectually too active to remain in the condition into which it was born, sharp-witted, cautious, provident, businesslike in every respect. This fact is frequently to be observed in poor families, where sloth and viciousness do not prevent its occurrence. But the man with moderate means, who in his household affairs must be continually regulating expenditure, has reason to believe that his children, especially the more mentally active of them, will grow up, unless great care is taken, into very unpractical views. The reasoning is something like this:
From earliest consciousness they will be thrown upon their own resources for enjoyment. The expensive toy, the luxurious recreation, will be entirely out of their reach. Yet, as the outwelling child-life must have some outlet, they will not be without toys, without recreation of one kind or another, and they will invent them for themselves. Out of the imagination they will fashion for themselves a domain where the simplest things have some rich meaning, glorified by an ideal excellence, and where all the most extravagant wishes are realized. In their plays they will be kings and queens of a garden-spot, transact weighty diplomatic business on the backs of old letters, and make boundless purchases of territory with pebbles or shells. In this cheap kingdom they will live as all-absorbing a life as the dignitaries whom they counterfeit live in theirs; and, still more, they will contract a bias very difficult to alter as increased years make it necessary. The boy who suddenly awakes to find himself a man finds it hard to believe that his old ideal efforts and ideal pleasures can not, by an elevation of their plane, be made sufficient for the satisfaction of a lifetime.

===Chinese===

正如那些千篇一律的生平传记所记载的那样，作为一个出身贫寒却德高望重家庭的孩子，那种被人们极度误以为能培育家庭养成务实习癖的严苛节俭家风，反倒将他的理想主义倾向滋养培育至登峰造极的精微之境。在家庭生计中处处精打细算的必然性，恰恰是最不可能培养出具有务实观念子女的土壤。毫无疑问，赤贫往往因激发了生存最原始的机能活动，使得那些在心智上过于敏锐而不甘屈居于出身境况的后代，演化得机敏过人、小心谨慎、未雨绸缪且在方方面面皆精明强干。在未曾受懒惰与恶德阻碍的贫困家庭中，此类事实屡见不鲜。然而，家境平平的小康之人——在持家度日中不得不时刻调控节制开支者——却有充沛的理由相信：其子女（尤其是其中智力更为活跃聪敏者），除非受到极其审慎的悉心管教，否则极易滋生出极不切实际的务虚观念。其推演理路大体如下：

自孩提时代最初萌生自我意识起，他们便唯有被迫依赖自身的内心世界去索求享乐。昂贵的玩具与奢华的消遣，于他们而言完全是遥不可及的奢望。然而，鉴于孩童澎湃涌动的生命活力必然需要寻觅宣泄的出口，他们断不会匮乏玩具，断不会缺少某种形式的嬉戏娱乐——他们会自己去发明创造。凭借天马行空的想象力，他们会为自己营构出一座专属的疆域：在彼处，最平凡简陋的物什皆承载着某种丰厚的意蕴，得蒙一种理想化卓越之光的升华荣耀，且一切最荒诞不经的奢望皆能如愿以偿。在嬉戏玩耍之中，他们化身为一片小小花园的国王与王后，在废弃旧信函的背面批阅处理着重大的外交事务，并用小鹅卵石或贝壳去大肆购置无边无际的浩瀚领土。在这座成本微薄的王国之中，他们沉浸其中的忘我程度，丝毫不亚于被他们所模仿伪托的达官显贵沉浸在真实显赫之中的模样；更有甚者，随着年岁渐长迫切需要做出改变之际，他们早已深植下一种极难扭转的性情偏向。那位在骤然惊醒时发觉自己已然长大成人的少年，极难相信自己早年间那些理想化的求索与欢愉，竟无法借由提升其境界层面而足以充当维系毕生满足的坚实依托。

===Original===

More particularly is this true when, as in the present age, the world of books offers an additional asylum to the active child, to which, unless, both in mind and money, he is very poor indeed, he may retreat for the enjoyment which an outer world does not supply. He is thus reared in an ideal atmosphere until it becomes the nutriment of his very being.
From such a state as this, and through his rough experience of human mercies at the hands of Dr. Bowyer, of Christ’s Hospital, we may follow Coleridge on till we find him at Cambridge. How little he was fitted by nature to cope with the stern substantialities of an English University course is to be read in his final abandonment of its honors under the pressure of pecuniary difficulties, and the despair of an impossible attachment, and his enlistment into the Dragoons as a desperate indication of a desperate state of mind. Then succeed the Pantisocracy, marriage without the means of a livelihood, editorship without patrons, and without a single natural qualification for the office except the proverbially unremunerative one of out-speaking sincerity; literary labors of all kinds, from the volume of poems to the political leader, travels upon a pension, communion with German mind in books and men, of all ideal things the most ideal.
At length, by these steps, with here and there a repetition, we reach the period when his opium life commenced. In all fairness, what sort of a training had his whole previous existence been for a calm looking at the dangers of the fascinating indulgence, for a rejection of its temptation?
I dare to affirm that there is many a man who, when jaded by the day’s labor, throws himself down to be refreshed by music, who in such an indulgence is committing no greater sin of intention than Coleridge committed when, coming weary from a lifetime, he abandoned himself to the enjoyment of that dangerous beauty which absolute necessities and spent vitality forbade him to look for in the external world. You and I, my reader, should we abandon ourselves to the opium indulgence, would know fully the wrong we were committing; Coleridge had not any definite idea. Bitterly did he repent it afterward; but his sorrow arose rather out of the terrible results of his course than from any self-recrimination, even in his sensitive mind, of malice aforethought.

===Chinese===

尤为真切的是：正如在当今时代，书卷的世界为思维敏捷的孩童提供了另一处避难所；除非他在心智与金钱两方面皆委实赤贫至极，否则他皆能退避其中，去索求外部世界所无法提供的享乐。他便是在这般理想主义的氛围之中被抚育成人，直至这种氛围彻底化作维系其自身存在的生命养分。

正是自这样一种境况出发，历经在基督公学（Christ’s Hospital）鲍耶博士（Dr. Bowyer）手下对人间温情的粗暴领教，我们得以追踪柯勒律治的足迹，直至目睹他踏入剑桥大学（Cambridge）。他在天性上是何等不适宜去周旋应付英国大学学业课程那冷酷严峻的实际事务，从其最终在经济窘迫的重压之下以及对一段不可能之恋情的绝望之中毅然放弃学术荣光、且作为其绝望心智状态的绝望表象毅然应征加入龙骑兵团的举动中，便昭然若揭。随后接踵而至的，是大同乌托邦构想，毫无糊口生计依托的婚姻，毫无资助庇护人的主编差事——且除了那出了名毫无报酬可言的直抒胸臆的赤诚之外绝无任何胜任该职位的天然禀赋；各色文学劳作，从诗集到政治社论，依靠年金资助的漫游旅行，在典籍与贤达中间同德意志心智的交融神会——凡此种种，皆属一切理想化事物中最崇高的理想。

最终，循着这些步履（偶伴随着前一步履的重演），我们抵达了他吸食鸦片生涯拉开帷幕的岁月节点。凭良心公允而论，在此前他的全部生命历程之中，何曾接受过任何足以让他平心静气审视这一迷人沉溺之危害、从而断然拒绝其诱惑的训练呢？

我敢断言：世间存在着许多这样的人，当其历经白昼的辛劳筋疲力竭之际，纵身仰卧任由音乐荡涤身心；而他在这种沉溺中所犯下的动机罪衍，绝不比柯勒律治所犯下的更为深重——彼时的柯勒律治带着一生的心力交瘁，将自己交付给那危险之美的享乐之中，而绝对的生存匮乏与枯竭殆尽的生机活力，早已严禁他在外部世界中寻觅那份美感。我的读者啊，你我倘若沉溺于鸦片之瘾，定会心知肚明自己正在犯下的过错；而柯勒律治彼时却绝无任何明确清晰的认知。日后他固然悔恨交加、痛心疾首；然而他的哀戚悲痛，更多的是源于其歧途所招致的惨烈恶果，而非源于在其敏感心智之中存在任何蓄意为恶的自责自咎。

===Original===

But whether, in the case of any opium-eater, the habit be or be not contracted with a full knowledge of its evil, there is but one view which we can take of the fruitlessness of struggles made for disenthrallment at a later period of his career. That fruitlessness is not to be treated with contempt as evidence of a cowardly lack of self-denial which prevents the man from breaking the meshes of a bondage grown delightful to him. We are called rather to look upon the agonies of one who, in a nightmare-dream of fearful precipices, has not the power of volition to draw himself from the edge; we must pity﻿—deeply pity. The protracted use of opium, not by any metaphor, but in a sense as rigorous as that of paralysis, utterly annihilates the power of will over action.
It is no mere cloak of apology which I would throw over those unfortunates who, after ineffectual attempts at being free, have subsided again into indulgence; it is actual fact that, in the horrors and the debility resulting from the disuse of the narcotic, its sufferers are no more responsible for their acts than the insane. When every man is a Scaevola, and can hold his hand in the flames till it is consumed, then may we expect men to endure the unrelieved tortures of opium-abandonment to their end in enfranchisement. Who of us would hold himself responsible for withdrawing his hand from the fire? I fancy the best of our martyrs, willing as they were to die for their cause, would have leaped out if they had not been chained among the fagots.
So far from extenuating the wrong of narcotics and stimulants, I believe myself only proclaiming (and I would it were with a thousand tongues) the perils into which they lead, as the most striking exponent of that wrong. This very emasculation of the will itself, while it may not produce the sensation of a detail of horrible visions, is in reality the most terrible characteristic of the injury wrought by these agents. A spiritual unsexing as it is, it vitiates all relations of life which exist to its victim; by submitting to it he sows a harvest of degradation, which involves in its mildewed sheaves manly fortitude, hopefulness, faith of promises, all the list of high-toned principles which are the virile﻿—yes, still more broadly﻿—the human glory.

===Chinese===

然而，就任何一名食鸦片者而言，无论其习癖是否是在充分知晓其罪恶的前提下养成的，对于其在日后生涯中为了挣脱羁绊所作出的徒劳抗争，我们唯能抱持一种视角。那种徒劳无功绝不可被轻蔑蔑视为懦弱缺乏克制自律的铁证——仿佛是懦弱阻碍了该人挣脱那对其已然演变为享乐的枷锁罗网。我们理当将其视作某人深陷万丈悬崖的噩梦幻境之中、却毫无意志之力将自己从悬崖边缘拉回的剧痛挣扎；我们必须同情怜悯——深切地怜悯。长期吸食鸦片，绝非借助任何比喻，而是在如同瘫痪中风一般严谨的生理意义上，彻底毁灭了意志对行动的掌控驾驭之力。

对于那些在屡次挣脱自由的徒劳尝试之后重新沉沦于恶瘾之中的不幸者，我并非企图为他们披上一件辩解开脱的外衣；铁一般的事实乃是：在停用麻醉剂所引发的无尽恐慌与虚弱衰竭之中，饱受折磨之人对其言行所承担的责任，绝不比疯癫狂人多出分毫。唯有当世间人人皆是斯凯沃拉（Scaevola）、能够将自己的手掌置于烈焰之中直至烧焦殆尽之际，我们方能苛求世人去忍受戒绝鸦片过程中毫无缓解的剧烈酷刑直至斩获解放。试问我们当中有谁会因自己本能地从烈火中抽回手掌而自责承担罪责？我设想，即便是我们最崇高的殉道者，纵然甘愿为信仰献出生命，倘若未曾被铁链紧锁在柴捆之中，亦定会本能地跃出火海。

我绝非企图开脱麻醉剂与兴奋剂的罪过，恰恰相反，我认为自己是在大声疾呼（我恨不得自己拥有千百条舌头去呼号）它们所引向的深渊绝境，将其作为彰显这一罪恶的最鲜明体现。这种对意志本身的彻底阉割扼杀，纵然或许未必会如详尽描摹可怖幻象那般带来感官上的震撼，实则却是此类药物所造成的摧残中最骇人听闻的本质特征。它乃是一场精神上的去势阉割，毒化了受害者赖以生存的全部生命纽带；通过向其屈服臣服，他播种下了一场堕落的浩劫收成——在那霉烂发臭的庄稼捆绑之中，卷入了大丈夫的坚毅气魄、希望、信守诺言的操守，以及构成雄性阳刚之美——是的，更宽泛而言——构成人类全部崇高荣光的一整张崇高原则清单。

===Original===

To this truth let a spirit so essentially noble as Coleridge witness, agonized by the shame of those subterfuges which were necessary sometimes to procure the indulgence that had become to him the very nutriment of his being.
It is vain for us to shut our eyes to the fact that opium-eating in all countries is an immense and growing evil. In America peculiarly it is so, from the constitution of our national mind. An intense devotion to worldly business in our representative man often coexists with a stifled craving for something higher. Beginning, for the sake of advancement, at an age when other nations are still in the playground or the schoolroom, he continues rising early and lying down late in the pursuit of his ambition to a period when they have retired to the ease of travel or a villa. Yet from the very fact that his fathers have done this before him, he inherits a constitution least of all fitted to bear those drafts upon it.
The question of his breaking down is only one of time. Sometimes it happens very early; and then not only does an exhausted vitality require to be replenished, but the long-pent-up craving for a beauty of which business activity has said, “It is not in me,” rises from its bonds, and, with a sad imperativeness, asks satisfaction.
How hard is it now to unlearn that habit of hasty execution which had been the acquirement of his whole previous life! The demands of business had always met from him with rapid dispatch; this complex craving must be answered as rapidly. The self-denial of recreation, abandonment of care, well-regulated regimen, might gradually restore to him health, and, with it, the elastic capacity for receiving happiness. He can not wait; the process is too slow. And the only immediate infusion of energy must be the artificial; the devil stands at his ear, and suggests opium. From that moment begins the sad, old, inevitable tale of the opium-eater’s life.
Alas! it is no rare one with us. The inhabitant of the smallest village need hardly go out of his own street to hear it, and the unknown wretched who hide their shame, first in sad family hearts, last in the unwhispering grave, are even more in number, doubtless, than the known.
The only effort which can be made by a man of good feeling to his race is to suggest some means of escape to those who feel their bondage.

===Chinese===

且让如柯勒律治这般本质崇高纯正的灵魂来见证这一真理吧——为了筹措换取那已然演变为其生命存续核心养分的恶瘾，他有时不得不诉诸种种卑劣的托词遁词，并为此饱受羞耻的噬心之痛。

对于食鸦片在所有国度皆是一项浩瀚且与日俱增的滔天祸害这一事实，我们对其熟视无睹乃是徒劳的。而在美国，源于我们国民心智的特有构造，这一祸害尤为显著深重。在我们具有代表性的典型人物身上，对世俗实务的极度全情投入，往往同一股被生生扼杀的对更高境界的渴盼并存。为了飞黄腾达出人头地，他在其余民族尚处于嬉戏操场或学堂书斋的幼小年岁便已然投身名利场，起早贪黑孜孜以求追逐其雄心壮志，直至其余民族早已退隐去安享漫游旅行或乡间别墅闲适的岁月阶段。然而，恰恰源于其父辈在他之前便早已如此行事的事实，他所继承的生理体魄，最不适宜去承受此类浩瀚的透支提款。

其身体心智的崩溃垮台仅仅是时间问题。有时这发生得极其早；届时，不仅枯竭耗尽的生命活力迫切需要得到补充填补，而且那股长期被压抑、关于商业实务曾宣称“我无法赋予”的对美的极度渴求，亦挣脱了枷锁羁绊，并以一种令人悲戚的专横霸道索求满足。

如今欲戒除那在他此前毕生岁月中养成的雷厉风行急躁习癖，该是何等艰难啊！实务操作的索求向来能在他手中斩获迅疾利落的处置；而这股错综复杂的渴求，亦必须以同样迅捷的节奏获得应答。克制舍弃享乐消遣、抛下操劳烦忧、恪守规律作息调理，本可循序渐进地使他重获康健，并随之重塑其接纳幸福的韧性潜能。然而他等不及；这套进程未免太慢。而唯一能够立竿见影注入能量的手段，必然是人工合成的造作力量；恶魔附在他的耳畔，窃窃私语着鸦片。自那一刻起，关于食鸦片者生命的古老、哀戚且无可避免的悲剧传说便拉开了序幕。

哀哉！在我们身边这绝非罕见的特例。即便最小村落里的村民，几乎无须走出自家街巷便能听闻此类悲剧；而那些将自身耻辱最初深藏在家庭哀痛的心坎里、最终深埋在沉寂无声坟墓之中的默默无闻的可怜受害者，其数量毫无疑问远胜于为人所知的那些典型。

一个对其同胞怀抱善意之人所能做出的唯一努力，便是为那些感知到自身受制于枷锁羁绊之人，指出一条逃离挣脱的生路。

===Original===

For the terror of beginners, enough both of precept and example has been diffused widely at the present day, if that would do any good. I would not be satisfying my convictions of right did I not add to any denunciation of the habit some index toward freedom; for I believe there are many men, perhaps some who will read these words, who would escape from the opium slavery at any expense of effort, provided that the lethal stupor of their energies could be removed. Where there is one man who, like De Quincey, can at last get free by his own unaided struggle, there are a thousand to whom help from without is an absolute necessity.
It was my happiness, very soon after breaking away from the hashish thraldom, to make the acquaintance of a gentleman whose experience of narcotics from eyewitness in their particular mother-countries, added to the capabilities which he possessed, as a medical man, for philosophizing upon such experience, interested me much in speaking with him. It had been his good fortune to meet with some singularly inordinate opium-eaters, who were in utter despair of recovery, and, still better, it was his blessing to effect a permanent and radical cure. In one case with which I became acquainted, the patient had reached a higher point of daily indulgence than De Quincey at his most desperate stage, and had seemingly lost all constitutional basis for restoration to work upon. Yet the restoration was effected. I owe it not less to a proper goodwill to humanity than to gratitude on the part of men to say who this physician was. Sincerely desirous of being in some way instrumental in the cure of a bondage which, if not my own, was, at least, so near akin to it that I can deeply sympathize with its oppressed, I give a name whose betrayal in these pages violates no secrecy to the public, while it may do a great good﻿—Dr. J. W. Palmer, of Roslyn, Long Island, the author-surgeon, late of the Honorable East India Company’s Service, and of “The Golden Dagon,” to which I have referred.2

===Chinese===

为了让初涉此道者望而生畏，当今世上已然广泛散布了充沛的训诫与前车之鉴——倘若这当真能产生任何实效的话。倘若在痛斥谴责这一恶瘾之余，我未能附上指引奔向自由的某种航标线索，我便绝无法满足内心对正义公理的坚定信念；因为我笃信世间存在着许多人——或许包括某些将读到这些文字的人——只要能涤除其生命能量中致命的麻木迟钝，他们甘愿不惜一切心血代价去挣脱鸦片的奴役枷锁。在世间，如德·昆西（De Quincey）这般最终能够凭借自身孤身一人的抗争斩获自由者不过一人，而迫切需要外部襄助者却有成千上万之众。

在挣脱大麻枷锁的羁绊后不久，我有幸结识了一位绅士；他在麻醉药物各自的原产国亲眼目睹的丰富经验，加之作为一名医者针对此类经验展开哲学思辨的卓绝才能，使得我在同他的交谈之中获益良多、兴致盎然。他曾有幸接触过数名沉溺程度极其离谱罕见、对康复痊愈彻底绝望的食鸦片者；且更为难能可贵的是，他得蒙眷顾达成了彻底且永久的治愈。在我所获知的一例案例中，该病患每日沉溺滥用的剂量甚至超越了德·昆西在最绝望境地下的巅峰剂量，且在体魄机能上看似已然彻底丧失了赖以实现康复生机的一切生理基石。然而，康复之功终究圆满达成。指出这位医者的尊姓大名，既是我对全人类所应尽的正当好意，亦是出于世人应有的感激之情。我诚挚期盼能以某种方式襄助治愈这样一种即便非我亲历、却与其如此一脉相承以至于我能对受其压迫者深切感同身受的枷锁羁绊，因此在此数页中透露出一个在公众面前绝无背弃任何保密承诺、却可能成就莫大善功的名号——长岛罗斯林（Roslyn, Long Island）的J. W. 帕尔默博士（Dr. J. W. Palmer），这位作家兼外科医官，曾效力于尊敬的不列颠东印度公司（Honorable East India Company），亦是我前文所引述的《金大衮》（The Golden Dagon）一书的著作者。2

## Endnotes / 尾注
===Original===

1. The work referred to is a monograph upon Trance and human Hybernation, by Dr. James Braid, of Edinburgh, and published by John Church, Princes Street, Soho, London. Besides the copy now in my hands, through the kindness of my friend Dr. Rosa, of Watertown, I have never seen any other, although it probably exists in medical libraries in this country. Aware, at any rate, that the book is inaccessible, except by considerable painstaking, to general readers, I will state the authority upon which the phenomenon of the fakir’s interment and trance is related, in order that it may rest upon a stronger basis of proof than the testimony of an exceedingly credulous and superstitious people like the natives of Lahore.
 Sir Claude Wade, formerly of her majesty’s service, and, at the date of Dr. Braid’s writing, residing in Ryde, on the Isle of Wight, assures the doctor by letter that he was present at Lahore during the period of the fakir’s inhumation, and witnessed his disinterment. By this gentleman, Sir C. E. Tervelyan, and Captain Osborne, all that is stated of the fakir by Dr. Braid is authenticated, and, indeed, through them did the doctor obtain the materials for his narrative.
 By as strong a conjunction of testimony, therefore, as could be desired for the proof of the most startling assertion, is this recital put beyond the possibility of being an imposture. ↩︎
2. Among a number of articles written at various times by this author upon the subject of the narcotic fascinations, is one, published some time ago over his own signature in the *New York Tribune*, relative to the employment of hashish in India both as a gratification and a remedy. My knowledge of his thorough acquaintance with the habits of the ultra Oriental people, among whom he so long dwelt, together with a number of astonishing cures of the opium bane which he effected when, as I have said, all hope of restoration seemed forever gone, makes me particularly desirous to give the article of which I speak in full, as supplementary, through its specific value, to that which I have written of my own experience of hashish. Except as an antispasmodic in a very limited number of diseases, the Cannabis is known and prized very little among our practitioners, and I am persuaded that its uses are far wider and more important than has yet been imagined.

===Chinese===

1. 所引用的著作系爱丁堡的詹姆斯·布雷德博士（Dr. James Braid）关于出神狂喜与人类冬眠假死的专题论著，由伦敦索霍区圣子街（Princes Street, Soho）的约翰·丘奇（John Church）出版。除了我手头承蒙沃特敦（Watertown）的挚友罗莎博士（Dr. Rosa）慨允馈赠的这一册之外，我从未见过其他副本，尽管它可能存在于本国的医学图书馆中。无论如何，鉴于普通读者若不费相当一番周折极难查阅该书，我在此特地引述叙述那位托钵僧（fakir）入土埋葬与出神假死现象所依凭的权威来源，以便使其立足于比拉合尔（Lahore）当地土著这般极度轻信盲从且迷信愚昧之人的证言更为坚实的证据基石之上。

曾效力于女王陛下政府的克劳德·韦德爵士（Sir Claude Wade），在布雷德博士著书之际居住在怀特岛的怀德（Ryde, on the Isle of Wight）；他致信向博士保证：在托钵僧入土掩埋期间他亲身身在拉合尔，且亲眼见证了将其掘出起土的全部过程。借由这位绅士、C. E. 特维利恩爵士（Sir C. E. Trevelyan）以及奥斯本上尉（Captain Osborne）的见证，布雷德博士关于该托钵僧的一切陈述皆获得了确凿证实；且事实上，正是通过他们，博士才斩获了撰写其叙述的第一手素材。

因此，凭借在证实最令人惊愕的断言时所能期盼的最强有力的确凿证言组合，这一叙述彻底排除了沦为一场骗局的可能。 ↩︎

2. 在该作者于不同时期针对麻醉沉溺课题所撰写的若干篇文章之中，有一篇于不久前由其亲自签署发表在《纽约论坛报》（*New York Tribune*）之上，论述了大麻在印度兼作为享乐物与药物的使用状况。鉴于我深知他对远东极端东方民族习俗的彻底熟稔（他曾在他们中间长期侨居），加之他在全盘康复的希望看似彻底破灭消逝之际所达成的数例令人惊叹的戒除鸦片祸害的治愈案例，使得我尤为期盼能将我所提及的该篇文章全文收录，通过其特有的专门价值，来为我自身关于大麻体验的著述提供补充。除了在极少数特定疾病中作为抗痉挛剂之外，在我们的执业医官中间，大麻很少为人所知晓与珍视，而我深信其功用远比世人迄今所构想的更为宽博重要。

===Original===

Urged by this conviction, I have therefore transcribed the article of Dr. Palmer, and offer it here to the thoughtful attention which it deserves from all, whether professional or lay, who wish to add a most beneficial agent to their pharmacopoeia. It is entitled

**Hashish In Hydrophobia**

To the Editor of the *New York Tribune*:

===Chinese===

深受这一信念的驱使，我因而抄录了帕尔默博士的文章，并在此将其呈献给所有期盼在自己的药典中增添一种最具裨益的药物之人——无论其为专业医者还是门外散客——该文理当获得他们深思熟虑的垂注。其标题为：

**疯狗狂犬病中的大麻**

致《纽约论坛报》主编：

===Original===

Sir﻿—In your journal of Friday last appeared a timely paper on hydrophobia, from Dr. Griscom, of the New York Hospital, being a report of the interesting case of Edward Bransfield, with the inevitably fatal termination. Allow me to add to the communication of Dr. Griscom another on the same subject, which may be deemed important. It is the result of medical observation in the East on the use and effects of hashish (*Cannabis Indica*). In thus writing for the public I shall avoid technicalities.
 The Radda and Coolee bazaars of the Black Town of Calcutta are the Borroboola-Ghas of heathendom﻿—the back slums of Budhism﻿—where the most abject of helots and a very Herod among cruel heathen are presented in the same person﻿—whither the flannel shirts and small-tooth combs of the Rev. Aminadab Sleek are sent every Friday night from Burton’s Theatre, but never reach. It is there you must go to procure your hashish fresh from the fields, and see your living subject try experiments on himself. If you have a lively case of Rabies in your compound, and carry a copy of *Monte Cristo* [For the benefit of those who have not read this novel of Dumas, let me say that in it quite a lively hashish vision is recorded] in your pocket, so much the better﻿—you are posted in the phenomena. You will find dirty, dreadful-looking shops, redolent of petroleum and the hubble-bubble [Indice for water-pipe], and prolific in Pariah dogs, ochre-colored urchins (which, as they flounder about on their bellies, always a shade or two lighter than the rest, oddly resemble young crocodiles), and every other living thing which should make those small-tooth combs lively in the market. And, amid these essentially Oriental surroundings, you will find a fat old gentleman, with the least possible clothing, to compromise between decency and the climate, who is either galvanic like Uriah Heep, or asleep like the Fat Boy, as you happen to catch him just before or after his pipe, and who is licensed to dispense to the denizens of that quarter charas, gunjah, and bhang, in the name of Lord Dalhousie, the most noble the Governor General in Council.
 At the season of flowering, a resinous substance exudes and concretes on the slender stalks, leaves, and tops of the hemp plant in India, a sticky gum which causes the young stems to adhere together tenaciously in the bundles of gunjah.

===Chinese===

先生——在贵报上周五的版面上，刊登了纽约医院格里斯科姆博士（Dr. Griscom）关于狂犬病（hydrophobia）的一篇适时论文，报告了爱德华·布兰斯菲尔德（Edward Bransfield）那一必然走向致命结局的引人注目的病例。且容我在格里斯科姆博士的通讯之外，就同一课题补充另一篇或许被视作至关重要的报告。这乃是在东方就大麻——即印度大麻（Cannabis Indica）——的功用与效应所进行的医学观察成果。在以此面向公众撰文之际，我将尽量避免使用专业术语。

加尔各答（Calcutta）黑人区的拉达与库利集市（Radda and Coolee bazaars），乃是异教世界里的波罗布拉-加荒蛮之地（Borroboola-Ghas）——是佛教世界背街幽暗的贫民窟——在彼处，最卑劣屈辱的贱民奴隶同残暴异教徒中最冷酷的希律王集于一人之身；伯顿剧院（Burton’s Theatre）每逢周五夜晚便向彼处运送阿米纳达布·斯里克牧师（Rev. Aminadab Sleek）的法兰绒衬衫与细齿篦子，却永远未能运抵目的地。欲寻觅刚从田野中采撷的新鲜大麻，并亲眼目睹鲜活的实验对象在自己身上施行实验，你便唯有涉足该地。倘若你的院落宅邸中恰好有一例活跃的狂犬病疯狗症（Rabies）病例，且衣袋里揣着一本《基督山伯爵》（*Monte Cristo*）［为了方便那些未曾读过大仲马这部小说的人，且容我说明：书中记载了一段相当生动逼真的大麻致幻景象］，那便再好不过了——你便已然对这些现象了然于胸。你会发觉那些肮脏不堪、面目可怖的店铺，弥漫着石油与水烟袋（hubble-bubble，印度语水烟筒）的刺鼻气味，四处乱窜着贱民野狗与赭色皮肤的顽童（当他们趴在肚皮上扑腾翻滚时，肚皮总比其余部位要浅上一两个色号，古怪地宛若一条条幼鳄），以及所有能让那些细齿篦子在集市上畅销走俏的活物。在这些极具东方风情的周遭环绕之中，你会撞见一位肥胖的老绅士，身上裹着尽量稀少的单薄衣衫以便在体面礼数与酷热气候之间达成妥协；依照你恰好在他吞云吐雾之前还是抽完烟斗之后撞见他，他要么如尤利亚·希普（Uriah Heep）一般神经质抽搐亢奋，要么如胖男孩（Fat Boy）那般呼呼大睡；他获准以枢密院总督达尔豪西勋爵（Lord Dalhousie）的名号，向那一街区的居民配售分发恰拉斯（charas）、甘贾大麻（gunjah）与邦格大麻（bhang）。

在开花季节，印度大麻植株纤细的茎秆、叶片与顶端会分泌并凝结出一种树脂质物质，这种黏稠的胶质使得甘贾大麻捆绑束中的嫩茎牢固顽固地黏附在一起。

===Original===

Men, now dressed all in leather, are sent into the fields to run to and fro, sweeping the plants with their garments, from which afterward they diligently gather the resin that has adhered. This is the charas, wherein is all the narcotic virtue of the herb, all the seventh heaven of hashish intoxication for the Hindu and the Arab. The most potent of it comes from Nepaul. Bhang, or *subjee*, is the larger leaves and capsules of the Cannabis compressed in balls and sticky layers, with here and there some flowers between. Infused with water, it forms an intoxicating brew, to which, however, the Hindus are not commonly addicted. Gunjah, mixed with tobacco and smoked in a pipe, is the shape of the drug which they popularly affect, and it is as gunjah that it is commonly sold in the shops. This comes in bundles, twenty-four of the plants entire, stalks, leaves, capsules, and tops undisturbed, and from which their resin has not been separated, adhering tenaciously. Gunjah, indeed, is the term proper to Hindustan, hashish being Arabic, and used to denote the tops and tenderest parts of the plant, sun-dried and powdered.
 Romantic extravagances have been written and told about the magic and the marvels of hashish, and Indian Coleridges and De Quinceys have been pressed into service to furnish forth characteristic stories for Oriental annuals and spectacles of the Monte Christo kind. These are for the most part fictitious, though, to be sure, your khidmatgar, if he happens to be a gunjah-wallah, is apt at times to indulge in splendid fancies, to make you a grand salaam instead of a sandwich, and offer you a houri when you merely demanded a red herring. But Dr.

===Chinese===

此时，身着全套皮革装束的男人们被派往田间来回奔跑，用衣衫擦拂植株，随后他们卖力地从衣物上收集黏附其上的树脂。这便是恰拉斯（charas），其中蕴含着该草本植物的全部麻醉精粹，蕴含着为印度人与阿拉伯人开启大麻沉醉第七重至高天的全部奥秘。其中药效最刚烈者来自尼泊尔（Nepaul）。邦格大麻（Bhang）或称“萨布吉（subjee）”，乃是大麻植株较大的叶片与蒴果压制捏成的团块与黏性薄层，其间夹杂着星星点点的花朵。将其浸泡于水，便酿成了一种致醉的饮品，然而印度人通常并不嗜好此道。甘贾大麻（Gunjah）掺杂烟草并在烟斗中吸食，乃是他们世俗普遍青睐的用药形式，且在店铺中通常亦是以甘贾大麻的形式出售。它是成捆出售的，整整二十四株完整植株，其茎秆、叶片、蒴果与顶端皆完好无损，其上的树脂未曾分离剥除，紧紧黏结依附在一起。事实上，“甘贾”乃是印度斯坦本土的专属称谓，“大麻（hashish）”则是阿拉伯语词汇，用于指代经日晒干燥并研磨成粉的植株顶芽与最娇嫩的部位。

关于大麻的魔力与神迹，世人曾写就并传颂了无数浪漫夸张的奇谈怪论，且印度版的柯勒律治与德·昆西们亦被拉来效力，为东方年刊与基督山式的奇观剧目炮制特色故事。这些大多纯属虚构杜撰；不过毫无疑问，倘若你的仆人（khidmatgar）恰巧是一介吸食甘贾者（gunjah-wallah），他有时确实极易沉溺于华丽灿烂的幻想之中：当你仅仅索要一份三明治时他会向你深施庄严的大礼（salaam），而当你仅仅索要一条红鲱鱼时他却向你奉上一位天堂仙女（houri）。然而——

===Original===

O’Shaugnessy, the present distinguished superintendent of the Indian telegraph, who formerly administered a model system of discipline among the native hospitals, and from his Eastern lookout has added here and there a new light to the firmament of science, who was the first to pursue this subject with well-directed researches, and procure from it definite results, describes the uniform effect of this agent on the human economy as consisting in a prompt and complete alleviation of pain; a singular power of controlling inordinate muscular spasms, especially in hydrophobia and traumatic tetanus; “as a soporific or hypnotic in conciliating sleep;” inordinate augmentation of appetite; the decided promotion of aphrodisiac desire; and sudden cerebral exaltation, with perfect mental cheerfulness, is in no case followed by the painful nervous “unstringing,” the constipation and suppression of secretions which attend the use of opium.
 Having daily under his eyes, in the streets of Calcutta, examples of this marvelous power of the gunjah, Dr. O’Shaughnessy proceeded, in a succession of judicious experiments, to apply it in several diseases attended with much muscular convulsion. Its action he discovered to be primarily on the motor nerves, promptly inducing complete loss of power in almost all the muscles; hence its timeliness in the spasms of tetanus, in the cramp of Asiatic cholera, in the sharp constriction of the muscles of deglutition in hydrophobia. In tetanus especially he met with signal success, even in his earliest experiments perfectly restoring ten cases in fourteen, and since then, to my personal knowledge, a still larger proportion. In the summer of 1852 it was administered with convincing success in cases of Asiatic cholera among the Company’s troops in Burma, even in the collapsed stage, subduing cramp and restoring warmth to the surface. Under its influence alone, that peculiar blueness and shriveling of the nails and fingers, familiarly known as “washerwoman’s hands,” has been rapidly dispersed, the flesh plumping out rosily again, like a decayed apple under an air-pump.

===Chinese===

——现任杰出的印度电报总监奥肖内西博士（Dr. O’Shaughnessy），昔日曾在土著医院中推行模范规章制度，且从其东方瞭望哨向科学的穹苍天宇增添了点点崭新明光；他是第一位以方向明确的严谨调研深究这一课题并从中斩获明确成果之人。他将这一药物对人体机能的恒常效应描述为：迅速且彻底抚平缓解剧痛；控制过激肌肉痉挛的奇异非凡力量，尤以狂犬病与创伤性破伤风为甚；“作为催眠剂或安眠药能够安抚诱导睡眠”；食欲的急剧异常大增；对催情欲望的显著促进；以及骤然的心智大脑亢奋伴随着全然的心境欢愉，且在任何情况下皆绝不会随之产生沉溺吸食鸦片所带来的痛苦神经“松弛溃散”、便秘以及分泌受阻。

每日在加尔各答的街巷中目睹甘贾大麻这般奇妙魔力的例证，奥肖内西博士在一连串严谨审慎的实验中，着手将其应用于数种伴有剧烈肌肉抽搐惊厥的疾病之中。他发觉其药理作用主要施加于运动神经，能迅速诱发几乎全部肌肉力量的彻底松弛丧失；因此，它在破伤风抽搐、亚洲霍乱痛性痉挛、以及狂犬病吞咽肌肉剧烈痉挛收缩中的应用具有极高的及时对症性。尤以破伤风为甚，他斩获了极其显著的辉煌成效，即便在他最早的实验中，十四例病例里便彻底治愈了十例，且自那以后，据我个人所知，治愈比例还要更高。在1852年夏季，在缅甸东印度公司驻军部队爆发的亚洲霍乱病例中，即便在患者陷入虚脱休克阶段，施用该药亦取得了令人信服的成效，成功压制了痛性抽搐，并使体表恢复了温热。单单在其影响之下，俗称为“洗衣妇之手”的那种指甲与指尖特有的青紫发绀与皱缩干瘪便迅速消散瓦解，肌肤重新红润丰满，宛若气泵之下的皱瘪苹果一般恢复饱满。

===Original===

Every intelligent physician will perceive that there is nothing in the kind of virtue manifested in these cases which has not a direct bearing, and by the same modus operandi, on the phenomena of hydrophobia, since it has been ably contended, especially in India, that the three diseases are of a kindred type; that their phenomena are purely nervous and functional, and that no local inflammations are necessary to their definition.
 In an occasional contribution to the British and Foreign Medical Review, and in some excellent monographs published in Calcutta, Dr. O’Shaughnessy has given the results of his experiments since 1850, by which it appears that in almost every case, with the Cannabis alone, he has succeeded in procuring perfect alleviation of pain, complete control of the spasm, and its attendant apprehension and infernal imagination﻿—indeed, an utter routing of all the horrors of the disease; and claiming, with a saving clause, one or two cures, he makes it evident that in every instance a painless, tranquil, conscious termination is attainable. His patients have swallowed water with avidity, paddled in it and made merry with it, and been friendly with it to the end.
 That it has thus overcome the horrors of Rabies and all the dreadfulness of such a deathbed, should procure for the Cannabis more consideration than it has met with at the hands of the profession in this country. The objection, hitherto valid, that its preparations are of unequal strength, and that the drug loses all its virtues by change of climate, is conclusively met and defeated at last by the admirable alcoholic extract of Mr. Robinson. The writer of this has seen a sepoy of the 40th Rifles, an hour before furiously hydrophobic, under the influence of the Cannabis not only drinking water freely, but pleasantly washing his face and hands.
 In conclusion, I would invoke for the *Cannabis Indica* the interest of American writers and practitioners by research and experiment.

J. W. Palmer, M.D.

↩︎

===Chinese===

每一位聪敏的医者皆能洞察：在这些病例中所彰显出的药效特质，借由完全相同的作用机制（modus operandi），与狂犬病的种种病理现象皆存在着直接关联，因为医学界早已提出过有力的论辩（尤以在印度为甚）：这三种疾病属于同一亲缘类型；其病理现象纯属神经性与功能性紊乱，界定它们断无须任何局部炎症的存在。

在向《英国与外国医学评论》（*British and Foreign Medical Review*）偶尔撰写的专稿以及在加尔各答出版的若干篇卓越专题论著中，奥肖内西博士给出了其自1850年以来的实验成果；从中可以看出，在几乎每一个病例中，单凭大麻一味药物，他便成功达成了剧痛的彻底抚平、痉挛抽搐以及伴随而来的惊恐战栗与地狱般恐怖妄想的全然遏制——事实上，是对该疾病全部恐怖症状的彻底击溃驱逐；且在附带保留条件的前提下断言治愈了一至两例，他雄辩地证明：在每一例病例中，皆完全有望达成无痛、安详、神志清醒的终末结局。他的病患们曾贪婪饥渴地大口吞饮清水，在水中嬉戏拍打且怡然自得，直至生命最后一刻皆能同清水相安无事。

大麻既然能以此种方式战胜狂犬病的无尽可怖与此类临终病榻的一切凄惨绝望，理当为它在本国同行医者手中赢得比以往更多的重视垂青。以往长期成立的反对意见——即其制剂药效强度参差不齐、且该药在气候环境变迁时会丧失全部药效——终于被罗宾逊先生（Mr. Robinson）那令人赞叹的醇提取物彻底击溃消解。本篇撰写者曾亲眼目睹第40步枪团的一名印度兵（sepoy），在一小时前尚且狂躁剧烈地惧水狂犬发作，在大麻的影响之下，不仅能够畅快自如地大口喝水，而且能够愉悦惬意地清洗面庞与双手。

综上所述，我期盼借由严谨的研究与实验，唤起美国著述学者与临床执业医官对印度大麻（Cannabis Indica）的殷切关注。

医学博士 J. W. 帕尔默（J. W. Palmer, M.D.）
